{\overline{N}}_{c}
colors and with the scale factor

\langle\Lambda_{L}(M)\rangle={\Lambda}_{SYM}
of its gauge coupling, and with the
frozen mion fields
M
. Finally, after
integrating out the dual gluons by means of the VY- procedure

[
15
]
, we obtain
the Lagrangian of mions

\displaystyle{\overline{K}}=\frac{1}{\Lambda_{Q}^{2}}\,{\rm Tr\,\Bigl(M^{\dagger}M\Bigr)}\,,\quad{\overline{{\mathcal{W}}}}=-{\overline{N}}_{c}\,\Biggl(\frac{\rm\det\,M}{\Lambda_{Q}^{\rm{b_{o}}}}\Biggr)^{1/{\overline{N}}_{c}}+m_{Q}{\rm Tr\,M}\,,\quad\mu\ll\Lambda_{SYM}\,\,.

(5.0.10)

It describes the mions
{\rm M}
with the masses

\displaystyle\mu_{M}\sim m_{Q}\Biggl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}^{2}}{M}\Biggr)\sim m_{Q}\Biggl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{m_{Q}}\Biggr)^{\frac{N_{F}-N_{c}}{N_{c}}}\,,\quad m_{Q}\ll\mu_{M}\ll\Lambda_{SYM}\,.

(5.0.11)

Clearly, there is no analog of these parametrically light
particles in the direct theory
.

6

Direct and dual theories. Unequal quark masses,

\mathbf{0<(2N_{F}-3N_{c})/N_{F}\ll 1,\,N_{c}<N_{\it l}<3N_{c}/2}

The standard consideration that allows ”to verify” that the duality
works properly
for quarks of unequal masses is as follows
[
5
,
2
]
. For
instance, we take
N_{\it h}
quarks of the direct theory to be heavier
than the other
N_{c}<N_{\it l}=N_{F}-N_{\it h}

quarks. Then, after integrating out these
\rm h
-flavored quarks as
heavy ones, the direct theory with
N_{c}
colors and
N_{\it l}
of
\it l
-flavors remains at lower energies. By duality, it is equivalent to
the dual theory with
N_{\it l}-N_{c}
colors and
N_{\it l}
flavors.
On the other side, the original theory is equivalent to the dual
theory with
{\overline{N}}_{c}=N_{F}-N_{c}
colors and
N_{F}
flavors. In this dual
theory, the
\rm h
-flavored dual quarks are
assumed
to be
higgsed, and hence the dual theory with
(N_{F}-N_{c}-N_{\it h})=N_{\it l}-N_{c}

colors and
N_{F}-N_{\it h}=N_{\it l}
of
\it l
-flavors remains at lower
energies.Therefore, all looks self-consistent.

But we consider now this variant in more details, starting from the
left end of the conformal window,
0<{\overline{\rm b}}_{0}/N_{F}\ll 1
.
At
\mu<\Lambda_{Q}
, the dual theory, by definition, has already entered
sufficiently deep the conformal regime and therefore
both its gauge and Yukawa couplings are close to their parametrically small
frozen values,
{\overline{a}}_{*}\sim a_{f}^{*}\sim(\rm{\overline{b}}_{o})/N_{F}\ll 1
. We take

N_{c}<N_{\it l}<3N_{c}/2
for the direct quarks
Q^{\it l}\,,{\overline{Q}}_{\it l}
to have smaller
masses
m_{\it l}
at
\mu=\Lambda_{Q}
, and other
N_{h}=N_{F}-N_{l}
quarks

Q^{\it h}\,,\,{\overline{Q}}_{\it h}
to have larger masses
m_{\it h}\,,\,\,r\equiv m_{\it l}/m_{\it h}\ll 1
.

6.1

Direct theory

We start from the direct theory, because, in a sense, its mass
spectrum is easier to calculate. It is in the strong coupling

a_{*}={\cal O}(1)
conformal regime at
m^{\rm pole}_{\it h}<\mu<\Lambda_{Q}
,
and the largest physical mass scale
\mu_{H}
is
here the pole mass of the heavier
Q^{\it h}\,,\,{\overline{Q}}_{\it h}
quarks :

\displaystyle\frac{m^{\rm pole}_{\it h}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\sim\frac{m_{h}}{z_{+}\Lambda_{Q}}\sim\frac{m_{\it h}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\,\Biggl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{m^{\rm pole}_{\it h}}\Biggr)^{\gamma_{Q}=\rm{b_{o}}/N_{F}\approx 1}\sim\Biggl(\frac{m_{\it h}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Biggr)^{(N_{F}/3N_{c})\approx 1/2}\,.

(6.1.1)

The competing gluon mass
\mu_{{\rm gl},\,l}
due to higgsing of

Q^{l}
quarks looks as follows (at not too large
N_{c}
, see
[
18
]
) :

\mu^{2}_{{\rm gl},\,l}\sim a(\mu_{{\rm gl},\,l})z^{\prime}_{+}\langle M_{l}\rangle,\,\,z^{\prime}_{+}\sim\Bigl(\frac{\mu_{{\rm gl},\,l}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\gamma_{Q}}\,\rightarrow\,\frac{\mu_{{\rm gl},\,l}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\sim\frac{\langle M_{l}\rangle}{\Lambda_{Q}^{2}}\sim r^{\frac{N_{l}-N_{c}}{N_{c}}}\Bigl(\frac{m_{H}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{1/2},\,\,\frac{\mu_{{\rm gl},\,l}}{m^{\rm pole}_{\it h}}\sim r^{\frac{N_{l}-N_{c}}{N_{c}}}\ll 1\,.

After integrating inclusively these quarks out, the lower energy
direct theory with
N_{c}
colors and

N_{c}<N_{\it l}<3N_{c}/2
flavors of lighter
Q^{\it l}\,,\,{\overline{Q}}_{\it l}
quarks remains.

7
7

7

To simplify all formulas, the value
(3N_{c}-2N_{\it l})/N_{\it l}
is considered
as
O(1)
quantity in this section,

From matching with the coupling
a_{*}=O(1)
of the higher energy
theory, its gauge coupling is also
O(1)
at
\mu=m^{\rm pole}_{\it h}
and
therefore the scale parameter of this gauge coupling is

\Lambda^{\prime}\sim m^{\rm pole}_{\it h}
. The
current masses of
Q^{\it l}\,,\,{\overline{Q}}_{\it l}
quarks at
\mu=m^{\rm pole}_{\it h}
are
\hat{m}_{\it l}\equiv m_{\it l}(\mu=m^{\rm pole}_{\it h})=r\,m^{\rm pole}_{\it h}\ll\Lambda_{Q}^{\prime}=m^{\rm pole}_{\it h},\,\,r\ll 1
.

Now, how to deal with this theory at lower energies ?  As was
described in
[
11
]
(see section 7 therein), there are two variants, ”a”
and ”b”. The heaviest masses
\mu_{H}
of the lower energy theory
at
\mu<\Lambda^{\prime}
are much smaller than
\Lambda^{\prime}
in the variant ”a”,
\mu_{H}\ll\Lambda^{\prime}
,
see section 5 above. At
\mu<\Lambda^{\prime}
this theory enters

smoothly
the strong coupling regime with
a({\Lambda}_{SYM}\ll\mu\ll\Lambda^{\prime})\gg 1
. Its mass spectrum is, in essence, the same
as those described above in section 5 and only the values of
N_{F}

and the scale factor of the gauge coupling are different :
N_{f}\rightarrow N_{l},\,\Lambda_{Q}\rightarrow\Lambda^{\prime}
. I.e., all
N_{l}
quarks and all gluons remain effectively
massless at scales
m^{\rm pole}_{l}<\mu\ll\Lambda^{\rm\prime}
. And
quarks are weakly confined, i.e. the tensions of confining stringis are
much smaller than their masses,
\sigma^{1/2}\sim{\Lambda}_{SYM}\ll m^{\rm pole}_{l}
. And only the gauge coupling is very large at

{\Lambda}_{SYM}<\mu<\Lambda^{\rm\prime},\,\,a(\mu)\gg 1
.
We therefore do not consider this variant ”a” in this section,
because our purpose here is to check the duality in the variant
most favorable for its validity.

This is the variant ”b”  :  ”confinement without chiral
symmetry breaking” at
m_{Q,l}\rightarrow 0

(although some general arguments have been
presented in section 7 of
[
11
]
that this variant cannot be
realized). This amounts
to assuming
(because all original

Q_{l},\,{\overline{Q}}_{l}
quarks and all gluons cannot ”dissolve in the
pure air”) that due to strong non-perturbative confining effects

\sim\Lambda^{\rm\prime}
the direct quarks
Q^{\it l}\,,{\overline{Q}}_{\it l}

and gluons form a large number of heavy hadrons with masses

\mu_{H}\sim\Lambda^{\rm\prime}
. Instead, new light
composite particles (special solitons) appear whose masses
are parametrically smaller than
\Lambda^{\rm\prime}
.
These are the dual quarks
{\hat{q}}_{i},\,{\hat{\overline{q}}}^{\,j},\,\,i,j=1...N_{l}
, the dual gluons with
{\overline{N}}_{c}^{\,\prime}=N_{\it l}-N_{c}
of dual colors, and the mions
{\hat{M}}_{l}\rightarrow{{\hat{M}}_{j}^{i}},\,\,i,j=1...N_{l}
. Their interactions at
scales
\mu<\Lambda_{Q}^{\prime}
are described by the Seiberg dual
Lagrangian. Their dual gauge coupling
{\overline{a}}(\mu)
at
\mu\sim\Lambda^{\prime}\sim m^{\rm pole}_{\it h}
is
{\overline{a}}(\mu=\Lambda^{\prime}/(\rm several))\sim{\it a}_{*}=O(1)
,
and therefore the scale parameter of this dual gauge
coupling is
\sim\Lambda^{\prime}\sim m^{\rm pole}_{\it h}
.

The dual Lagrangian (at the scale
\mu\sim\Lambda^{\prime}\,,\,\langle{\overline{Q}}^{\it l}Q_{\it l}(\mu=\Lambda_{Q})\rangle=\langle S\rangle/m_{\it l}={\Lambda}_{SYM}^{3}/m_{\it l}
) is

\displaystyle\overline{K}={\rm Tr\,}_{\it l}\bigl({\hat{q}}^{\dagger}{\hat{q}}+{\hat{\overline{q}}}^{\dagger}{\hat{\overline{q}}}\bigr)+\frac{1}{(\Lambda^{\prime})^{2}}{\rm Tr}_{l}\bigl({\hat{M}}^{\dagger}_{l}{\hat{M}}_{l}\bigr)\,,

(6.1.2)

{\overline{{\mathcal{W}}}}=\frac{2\pi}{{\overline{\alpha}}(\Lambda^{\prime})}\,{\overline{S}}-\frac{1}{\Lambda^{\prime}}\,{\rm Tr}_{\it l}\Bigl({\hat{\overline{q}}}\,{\hat{M}}_{\it l}\,{\hat{q}}\Bigr)+{\hat{m}_{\it l}}\,{\rm Tr}\,{\hat{M}}_{\it l}\,;\quad{\overline{S}}={\overline{W}}_{\beta}^{2}/32\pi^{2}\,,\quad\langle{\hat{\overline{q}}}^{\it j}\,{\hat{q}}_{\it i}\rangle=\delta_{\it i}^{\it j}\,{\hat{m}}_{\it l}\,{\Lambda^{\prime}}\,,

\displaystyle\hat{m}_{\it l}\equiv m_{\it l}(\mu=\Lambda^{\,\prime})=z_{+}^{-1}m_{\it l}\,,\,\,\langle{\hat{M}}_{\it l}\rangle=\,z_{+}\langle M_{\it l}\rangle,\,\,\,z_{+}\sim\Biggl(\frac{m^{\rm pole}_{\it h}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Biggr)^{\gamma_{Q}=\frac{\rm{b_{o}}}{N_{F}}\approx 1}\sim\Biggl(\frac{\Lambda^{\prime}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Biggr)\ll 1\,.

(6.1.3)

This dual theory is in the IR free weakly coupled HQ-phase at

\mu<\Lambda^{\prime}
. At lower scales
\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,\,\it l}<\mu<\Lambda^{\,\prime}
both its gauge and Yukawa couplings
decrease logarithmically (
\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}^{\,\prime}=3{\overline{N}}_{c}^{\,\prime}-N_{\it l}<0
) and become much less than unity at

\mu=\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,\,\it l}\ll\Lambda^{\,\prime}
and
r\ll 1
. The perturbative
pole mass
\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,\,\it l}
of quarks
{\hat{q}}^{\it l},\,{\hat{\overline{q}}}_{\it\overline{l}}
is (we ignore below logarithmic RG-evolution factors):

\displaystyle\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,\,\it l}\sim{\hat{\mu}_{q,\,\it l}}\sim z_{+}\Bigl(\frac{\langle M_{l}\rangle}{\Lambda^{\prime}}\Bigr).

(6.1.4)

At scales
\mu<\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,\,\it l}
all quarks
{\hat{q}}^{\it l},\,{\hat{\overline{q}}}_{\it\overline{l}}
can be integrated out inclusively as heavy ones,
and there remains the dual SYM theory with
{\overline{N}}_{c}^{\,\prime}=N_{\it l}-N_{c}
colors and mions
{\hat{M}}_{\it l}
. The scale factor

{\tilde{\Lambda}_{SYM}}=\langle{\overline{\Lambda}}_{L}({\hat{M}}_{\it l})\rangle
(with mions
{\hat{M}}_{\it l}
sitting down on
{\overline{\Lambda}}_{L}({\hat{M}}_{\it l}
) ) of its gauge coupling is determined
from the matching , see
[
11
]
and (
3.0.4
)

3{\overline{N}}_{c}^{\,\prime}\ln\Biggl(\frac{\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,\,\it l}}{-{\tilde{\Lambda}}_{SYM}}\Biggr)=\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}^{\,\prime}\ln\Biggl(\frac{\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,\,\it l}}{\Lambda^{\,\prime}}\Biggr)\quad\rightarrow

\displaystyle-{\tilde{\Lambda}}_{SYM}=\langle{\overline{\Lambda}}_{L}({\hat{M}}_{\it l})\rangle={\Lambda}_{SYM}=\Biggl(\Lambda_{Q}^{\rm{b_{o}}}m_{\it l}^{N_{\it l}}m_{\it h}^{N_{\it h}}\Biggr)^{1/3N_{c}}\,.

(6.1.5)

Applying now the VY procedure to integrate out dual gluons, we obtain
the lowest-energy Lagrangian of mions
{\hat{M}}_{\it l}
at
\mu<{\Lambda}_{SYM}
:

\displaystyle K_{M}\sim\frac{1}{(\Lambda_{Q}^{\,\prime})^{2}}{\rm Tr}\Bigl({\hat{M}}_{l}^{\dagger}{\hat{M}}_{l}\Bigr)\,\quad{\mathcal{W}}=\Biggl[\,\,-(N_{\it l}-N_{c})\,\Biggl(\,\frac{\det{\hat{M}}_{\it l}}{(\Lambda^{\,\prime})^{(3N_{c}-N_{\it l})}}\,\Biggr)^{1/(N_{\it l}-N_{c})}+\hat{m}_{\it l}\,{\rm Tr}\hat{M}_{\it l}\,\,\Biggr]\,,

(6.1.6)

From (
6.1.6
), the masses of mions
{\hat{M}}_{\it l}
are

\displaystyle\mu({\hat{M}}_{\it l})\sim m_{\it l}\,\frac{\Lambda_{Q}^{2}}{\langle{M}_{\it l}\rangle}\sim\,\Biggl(r=\frac{m_{\it l}}{m_{\it h}}\Biggr)^{\frac{2N_{c}-N_{\it l}}{N_{c}}}\,\,m^{\rm pole}_{\it h}\,,\quad r\ll 1\,,

(6.1.7)

\frac{{\Lambda}_{SYM}}{\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,\,\it l}}\sim\,\,r^{\Delta}\ll 1\,,\quad\frac{\mu({\hat{M}}_{\it l})}{{\Lambda}_{SYM}}\sim\,r^{2\Delta}\,\ll 1\,,\quad 0<\Delta=\frac{3N_{c}-2N_{\it l}}{3N_{c}}<\frac{1}{3}\,.

Therefore, on the whole for Section 6.1, when we started from the
direct theory in the conformal region, then integrated out the
heaviest
Q^{h}\,,{\overline{Q}}_{\it h}
quarks
at
\mu=m^{\rm pole}_{\it h}
(
6.1.1
) (weakly confined
by strings with the tension
\sigma^{1/2}\sim{\Lambda}_{SYM}\ll m^{\rm pole}_{\it h})
and,
in the variant ”b” (i.e. ”confinement without chiral symmetry
breaking” in the chiral limit, see section 7 in
[
11
]
), dualized
the remained theory of
Q^{\it l},\,{\overline{Q}}_{\it l}
- flavors,
the mass spectrum is as follows.

1)  There is a large number of heavy direct
{\it h}
-mesons

M^{\rm dir}_{\it hh}
, made of original
Q^{h}\,,{\overline{Q}}_{\it h}
quarks
connected by strings with the small tension
\sigma^{1/2}\sim{\Lambda}_{SYM}
,
with masses
\sim m^{\rm pole}_{\it h}\sim\Lambda^{\rm\prime}
, see (
6.1.1
).

\mathbf{1}^{\prime}
)   In the variant ”b” considered here, the dualization
of
Q^{\it l}\,,{\overline{Q}}_{\it\overline{l}}
quarks and direct gluons leaves behind a
number of
M^{\rm dir}_{\it ll}
mesons made of
Q^{\it l}\,,{\overline{Q}}_{\it l}

quarks connected by strings with the tension
\sigma\sim\Lambda^{\rm\prime}
,
with masses
\sim\Lambda^{\rm\prime}\sim m^{\rm pole}_{\it l}
. Besides, there is also a number
of baryons
B_{\it l},\,B_{\it lh}
and gluonia, all also with masses
\sim\Lambda^{\rm\prime}
.

All other pure dual particles have smaller masses and originate from
dual quarks
{\hat{q}}^{\it l}\,,{\hat{\overline{q}}}_{\it\overline{l}}
, dual
gluons and mions
{\hat{M}}_{\it l}
.

2) There is a large number of
l
-flavored dual mesons
and
b_{\it l}
-baryons made of nonrelativistic and weakly confined
(the dual string tension is
\sqrt{\sigma}\sim{\Lambda}_{SYM}\ll\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,\,\it l}\ll m^{\rm pole}_{\it h}
)

{\hat{q}}^{\it l}\,,{\hat{\overline{q}}}_{\it\overline{l}}
quarks, with their pole
masses

\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,\,\it l}\sim(r)^{(N_{\it l}-N_{c})/N_{c}}\,m^{\rm pole}_{\it h}\sim(1/r)^{(3N_{c}-2N_{\it l})/3N_{c}}{\Lambda}_{SYM}

.

3) There is a large number of gluonia with masses
\sim{\Lambda}_{SYM}\ll\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,\,\it l}
.

4) The lightest are
N_{\it l}^{2}
scalar mions
{\hat{M}}_{\it l}

with masses
\mu({\hat{M}}_{\it l})\sim(r)^{2\Delta}\,{\Lambda}_{SYM}\ll{\Lambda}_{SYM}
.

6.2

Dual theory

We return to the beginning of this section and start directly from
the dual theory
with
SU({\overline{N}}_{c})
dual colors,
N_{F}
dual quarks
q
and
\overline{q}
, and

N_{F}^{2}
mions
M_{i}^{j}
. At the scale
\mu<\Lambda_{Q}
the theory is
already entered the weak coupling conformal regime, i.e. its coupling

{\overline{a}}(\mu)
is close to
{\overline{a}}_{*}={\overline{N}}_{c}\,{\overline{\alpha}}_{*}/2\pi\approx 7\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}/3{\overline{N}}_{c}\ll 1
, see footnote
5

in section 3. We first consider the case most favorable for the dual
theory, where the parameter
r=m_{\it l}/m_{\it h}
is already taken to be
sufficiently small (see below). Then, the highest physical mass scale

\mu_{H}
in the dual theory is determined by masses of dual gluons due
to higgsing of
{\overline{q}}^{\it h},\,q_{\it h}
quarks :

\mu_{H}^{2}={\overline{\mu}}_{gl,\,\it h}^{2}\sim{\overline{a}}_{*}\langle{\overline{q}}^{\it h}q_{\it h}(\mu={\overline{\mu}}_{gl,\,\it h})\rangle.

. The mass spectrum of the dual theory in this
phase can be obtained in a relatively standard way, similarly to

[
11
,
20
]
. For this reason, we will skip from now on some
intermediate relations in similar situations in what follows. The
emphasis will be made on new elements that have not appeared before.

1)    The masses of
N_{\it h}(2{\overline{N}}_{c}-N_{\it h})
massive dual gluons and
their scalar superpartners are

\displaystyle\Bigl({{\overline{\mu}}_{gl,\,\it h}}\Bigr)^{2}\sim\langle{\overline{q}}^{\it h}q_{\it h}\rangle\Biggl(\frac{{\overline{\mu}}_{gl,\,\it h}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Biggr)^{\gamma_{q}}\sim\mu_{1}m_{\it h}\Biggl(\frac{{\overline{\mu}}_{gl,\,\it h}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Biggr)^{\gamma_{q}}\sim Z_{q}\Lambda_{Q}m_{\it h}\Biggl(\frac{{\overline{\mu}}_{gl,\,\it h}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Biggr)^{\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}/N_{F}}\,,

(6.2.1)

\displaystyle\frac{{\overline{\mu}}_{gl,\,\it h}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\sim\exp\{-{\overline{N}}_{c}/14\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}\}\,\Biggl(\,\frac{m_{\it h}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Biggr)^{N_{F}/3N_{c}}\ll\Biggl(\,\frac{m_{\it h}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Biggr)^{(N_{F}/3N_{c})\approx 1/2}\,,\quad{\it Z_{q}}\sim\exp\{-{\overline{N}}_{c}/7\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}\}\ll 1\,.

(6.2.2)

2)    The
N_{\it l}N_{\it h}
hybrid mions
M_{\it hl}
and
N_{\it l}N_{\it h}
nions

N_{\it lh}
(these are those dual
{\it l}
-quarks that have higgsed
colors, their partners
M_{\it lh}
and
N_{\it hl}
are implied and are
not shown explicitly) can be treated independently of other degrees of
freedom, and their masses are determined mainly by their
common mass term in the superpotential,

\displaystyle K_{\rm hybr}\simeq{\hat{z}}_{M}\,{\rm Tr}\,\Biggl(\frac{M^{\dagger}_{\it hl}M_{\it hl}}{Z^{2}_{q}\Lambda_{Q}^{2}}\Biggr)+{\hat{z}}_{q}\,{\rm Tr}\,\Biggl(N^{\dagger}_{\it lh}N_{\it lh}\Biggr)\,,\quad{\overline{{\mathcal{W}}}}=\Bigl(Z_{q}m_{\it h}\Lambda_{Q}\Bigr)^{1/2}{\rm Tr}\,\Biggl(\frac{M_{\it hl}N_{\it lh}}{Z_{q}\Lambda_{Q}}\Biggr)\,,

(6.2.3)

where
{\hat{z}}_{q}
and
{\hat{z}}_{M}
are the perturbative
renormalization factors of dual quarks and mions,

{\hat{z}}_{q}={\hat{z}}_{q}(\Lambda_{Q},\,{\overline{\mu}}_{gl,\,\it h})\sim\Biggl(\frac{{\overline{\mu}}_{gl,\,\it h}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Biggr)^{\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}/N_{F}}\sim\Biggl(\frac{m_{\it h}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Biggr)^{\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}/3N_{c}}\ll 1\,,\quad{\hat{z}}_{M}={\hat{z}}_{M}(\Lambda_{Q},\,{\overline{\mu}}_{gl,\,\it h})=1/{{\hat{z}}_{q}}^{2}\gg 1\,.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\frac{\mu(M_{\it hl})}{\Lambda_{Q}}\sim\frac{\mu(N_{\it lh})}{\Lambda_{Q}}\sim\exp\{-{\overline{N}}_{c}/14\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}\}\,\Biggl(\,\frac{m_{\it h}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Biggr)^{N_{F}/3N_{c}}\sim\frac{\it{\overline{\mu}}_{gl,\,\it h}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\,.

(6.2.4)

3)   Because the
{\overline{q}}^{\it h}
and
q_{\it h}
quarks are
higgsed,
N_{\it h}^{2}
pseudo-Goldstone
mesons
N_{\it hh}
(nions) appear. After integrating out the
heavy gluons and their superpartners, the
Lagrangian of remained degrees of freedom takes the form

K\simeq{\hat{z}}_{M}{\rm Tr}\Biggl(\frac{M^{\dagger}_{\it hh}M_{\it hh}+M^{\dagger}_{\it ll}M_{\it ll}}{Z^{2}_{q}\Lambda_{Q}^{2}}\Biggr)+\,{\hat{z}}_{q}\,2\,{\rm Tr}\sqrt{N^{\dagger}_{\it hh}N_{\it hh}}\,+{\hat{z}}_{q}{\rm Tr\,}_{\it l}\,\Biggl((\hat{q})^{\dagger}{\hat{q}}+{\hat{q}}\rightarrow{\hat{\overline{q}}}\Biggr)\,,

\displaystyle{\mathcal{W}}=\frac{2\pi}{\overline{\alpha}^{\,\prime}(\mu)}{\overline{S}}^{\,\prime}+{\rm Tr}\,\Biggl(\frac{M_{\it hh}N_{\it hh}}{Z_{q}\Lambda_{Q}}\Biggr)-{\rm Tr\,}_{\it l}\Biggl(\frac{{{\hat{\overline{q}}}}\,M_{\it ll}\,{\hat{q}}}{Z_{q}\Lambda_{Q}}\Biggr)+\,{\rm Tr}\,\Biggl(m_{\it l}M_{\it ll}+m_{\it h}M_{\it hh}\Biggr)\,,

(6.2.5)

where
{\overline{S}}^{\,\prime}
includes the field strengths of the
remaining
SU({\overline{N}}_{c}^{\,\prime})
dual gluons with
{\overline{N}}_{c}^{\,\prime}={\overline{N}}_{c}-N_{\it h}=N_{\it l}-N_{c}
dual colors, and
{\hat{q}}^{l},\,{\hat{\overline{q}}}_{\it\overline{l}}

are still active
l
- flavored dual quarks
with not higgsed colors and, finally, the nions
N_{\it hh}
are
”sitting down” inside
{\overline{\alpha}}^{\,\prime}(\mu)
.

At lower scales
\mu<{\overline{\mu}}_{gl,\,\it h}
, the mions
M_{\it hh}
and nions
N_{\it hh}
are frozen and don’t evolve, while the gauge coupling
decreases logarithmically in the interval
{\overline{\mu}}^{\rm pole}_{q,\,\it l}<\mu<{\overline{\mu}}_{gl,\,\it h}
. The
numerical value of the pole mass of
{\hat{\overline{q}}}_{\it l}\,,\,{\hat{q}}^{\it l}
- quarks is

\displaystyle\frac{{\overline{\mu}}^{\rm pole}_{q,\,\it l}}{\Lambda_{Q}}=\frac{\langle M_{\it ll}\rangle}{Z_{q}\Lambda_{Q}^{2}}\,\frac{1}{{\hat{z}}}_{q}\sim\exp\Bigl\{\,\frac{{\overline{N}}_{c}}{7\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}}\,\Bigr\}\,\Biggl[\,\Bigl(r\Bigr)^{\frac{N_{\it l}-N_{c}}{N_{c}}}\,\Biggl(\frac{m_{\it h}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Biggr)^{N_{F}/3N_{c}}\,\Biggr]\,,

(6.2.6)

z^{\,\prime\prime}_{q}=z^{\,\prime}_{q}({\overline{\mu}}_{gl,\,\it h}\,,{{\overline{\mu}}^{\rm pole}_{q,\,\it l}})\simeq z^{\,\prime}_{q}(m^{\rm pole}_{\it h}\,,\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,\,\it l})=z^{\,\prime}_{q}\,,

ignoring the   additional logarithmic renormalization factor

z^{\,\prime\prime}_{q}\ll 1
of active l-quarks.

After integrating out the quarks
\hat{\overline{q}}_{\it l}\,,\,\hat{q}^{\it l}
as heavy
ones at
\mu<{\overline{\mu}}^{\rm pole}_{q,\,\it l}
, the dual
SU(N_{\it l}-N_{c})
Yang-Mills
theory remains with the scale factor of its gauge coupling
\langle-{\overline{\Lambda}}_{L}\rangle={\Lambda}_{SYM}
(and with nions
N_{\it hh}

and mions
M_{\it ll}
”sitting down” on
{\overline{\Lambda}}_{L}
). Finally,
integrating out the dual gluons via the VY procedure, we obtain
( recalling that all fields in (
6.2.5
) and (
6.2.7
)
are normalized at
\mu=\Lambda_{Q}
, and
{\Lambda}_{SYM}/\Lambda_{Q}=(r)^{N_{\it l}/3N_{c}}\,(m_{\it h}/\Lambda_{Q})^{N_{F}/3N_{c}}\,,\,\,\langle N_{\it hh}\rangle=\langle{\overline{q}}q\rangle=Z_{q}m_{\it h}\Lambda_{Q}
)

\displaystyle K={\hat{z}}_{M}{\rm Tr}\Biggl(\frac{M^{\dagger}_{\it hh}M_{\it hh}+M^{\dagger}_{\it ll}M_{\it ll}}{Z^{2}_{q}\Lambda_{Q}^{2}}\Biggr)+\,{\hat{z}}_{q}\,2\,{\rm Tr}\sqrt{N^{\dagger}_{\it hh}N_{\it hh}}\,,

(6.2.7)

{\mathcal{W}}={\rm Tr}\,\Biggl(\frac{M_{\it hh}N_{\it hh}}{Z_{q}\Lambda_{Q}}\Biggr)-(N_{\it l}-N_{c})\,{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{3}\Biggl(\det\frac{\langle N_{\it hh}\rangle}{N_{\it hh}}\det\frac{M_{\it ll}}{\langle M_{\it ll}\rangle}\Biggr)^{1/(N_{\it l}-N_{c})}+\,{\rm Tr}\,\Biggl(m_{\it l}M_{\it ll}+m_{\it h}M_{\it hh}\Biggr)\,,

where

z^{\,\prime\prime}_{M}=z^{\,\prime\prime}_{M}({\overline{\mu}}_{gl,\,\it h}\,,{{\overline{\mu}}^{\rm pole}_{q,\,\it l}})\simeq z^{\,\prime}_{M}(\mu_{\it gl,\,h}\,,{\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,\,\it l}})=z^{\,\prime}_{M}\gg 1
is the
logarithmic renormalization factor of
M_{\it ll}
mions was ignored

8
8

8

The second term of the superpotential in (
6.2.7
) can
equivalently be written as :

-(N_{\it l}-N_{c})\,\Biggl(\frac{\det{M_{\it ll}}}{\Lambda_{Q}^{\rm{b_{o}}}\,{\det\Bigl(N_{\it hh}/Z_{q}\Lambda_{Q}\Bigr)}}\Biggr)^{1/(N_{\it l}-N_{c})}

The masses obtained from (
6.2.7
) look then as follows :

\displaystyle\frac{\mu(M_{\it hh})}{\Lambda_{Q}}\sim\frac{\mu(N_{\it hh})}{\Lambda_{Q}}\sim\Biggl({\hat{z}}_{q}\frac{|\langle N_{\it hh}\rangle|}{\Lambda_{Q}^{2}}\Biggr)^{1/2}\sim\exp\Bigl\{-{\overline{N}}_{c}/14\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}\Bigr\}\,\Biggl(\,\frac{m_{\it h}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Biggr)^{N_{F}/3N_{c}}\sim\frac{\it{\overline{\mu}}_{gl,\,\it h}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\,,

(6.2.8)

\frac{\mu(M_{\it ll})}{\Lambda_{Q}}\sim Z_{q}^{2}\frac{m_{\it l}\,\Lambda_{Q}}{{\hat{z}}_{M}\langle M_{\it ll}\rangle}\sim\exp\Bigl\{-\frac{2{\overline{N}}_{c}}{7\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}}\Bigr\}\Bigl(r\Bigr)^{\frac{2N_{c}-N_{\it l}}{N_{c}}}\Biggl(\frac{m_{\it h}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Biggr)^{N_{F}/3N_{c}}\,.

On the whole for this Section 6.2, when we started directly from the
dual theory with

{\overline{N}}_{c}=(N_{F}-N_{c})
colors,
N_{F}=(N_{\it l}+N_{\it h})
quarks
\overline{q}\,,q
and

N_{F}^{2}
mions
M^{i}_{j}
, its mass spectrum is as follows.

1)  The sector of heavy masses includes :  a)

N_{\it h}(2{\overline{N}}_{c}-N_{\it h})
of massive dual
gluons and their scalar superpartners;  b)
2N_{\it l}N_{\it h}

hybrid scalar mions
M_{\it hl}+M_{\it lh}
,  c)
2N_{\it l}N_{\it h}
hybrid
scalar nions
N_{\it lh}+N_{\it hl}
(these are
q^{\it l}\,,\overline{q}_{\it l}
quarks with higgsed colors),  d)
N_{\it h}^{2}
scalar
mions
M_{\it hh}
and
N_{\it h}^{2}
of scalar nions
N_{\it hh}
. All
these particles, with
specific numbers of each type, definite
spins, and other quantum numbers
, have definite masses of
the same order

\sim{\overline{\mu}}_{gl,\,\it h}\sim\exp\{-{\overline{N}}_{c}/14\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}\}(m_{\it h}/\Lambda_{Q})^{N_{F}/3N_{c}}\Lambda_{Q}\sim

\exp\{-{\overline{N}}_{c}/14\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}\}\,m^{\rm pole}_{\it h}\ll m^{\rm pole}_{\it h}\sim(1/r)^{N_{\it l}/3N_{c}}{\Lambda}_{SYM}\,.

All these particles are
interacting only weakly
, with both
their gauge and
Yukawa couplings
{\overline{a}}_{*}\sim a_{f}^{*}\sim\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}/N_{F}\ll 1
.

1’)  Because the dual quarks
q^{\it h}\,,{\overline{q}}_{\it h}

are higgsed, one can imagine that solitonic excitations also appear in
the form of monopoles of the dual gauge group (its broken part), see
e.g. section 3 in
[
20
]
and footnote 6 therein.
These dual monopoles are then confined and can, in principle, form a
number of additional hadrons
H_{\it h}^{\,\prime}
. Because the dual
theory is weakly coupled at the scale of higgsing,
\mu\sim{\overline{\mu}}_{gl,\,\it h}\sim\exp\{-{\overline{N}}_{c}/14\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}\}\,m^{\rm pole}_{\it h}
, the masses of these monopoles, as well as
th tension
\sqrt{\,\overline{\sigma}}
of strings confining them (with our
exponential accuracy in parametrical dependence on
{\overline{N}}_{c}/\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}\gg 1
),
are also
\sim{\overline{\mu}}_{gl,\,\it h}
. Therefore, the mass scale of these hadrons

H_{\it h}^{\,\prime}
is also
\sim{\overline{\mu}}_{gl,\,\it h}\ll m^{\rm pole}_{\it h}
. We even assume
(in favor of the duality) that, with respect to their quantum
numbers,   these hadrons
H_{\it h}^{\,\prime}
can be identified in
some way with the direct hadrons made of the quarks
{\overline{Q}}^{\it h}\,,Q^{\it h}
. But even then, the masses of
H_{\it h}^{\,\prime}
are
parametrically smaller
than those of various direct hadrons made of
the
{\overline{Q}}^{\it h}\,,\,Q^{\it h}
quarks ,
\mu(H^{\,\prime}_{\it h})\sim\exp\{-{\overline{N}}_{c}/14\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}\}\,m^{\rm pole}_{\it h}\ll m^{\rm pole}_{\it h}
.

Besides, the chiral symmetry of
\,{\it\overline{l}}\,,\,\it l
flavors
and the
R
-charges of the lower energy theory at
{\overline{\mu}}^{\rm pole}_{q,\,\it l}<\mu<{\overline{\mu}}_{gl,\,\it h}

remain unbroken
and the
\it l
- flavored dual quarks are
not higgsed and remain effectively massless in this
interval of scales. Therefore, no possibility is seen in this dual
theory in Section 6.2 for the appearance of
\it l
-flavored chiral
hadrons
H^{\,\prime}_{\it l}
(mesons and
baryons) with the masses
\sim{\overline{\mu}}_{gl,\,\it h}
that could be identified
with a large number of various direct
{\it l}
- flavored chiral
hadrons (mesons and baryons) made of
{\overline{Q}}^{\it l}\,,Q_{\it l}

quarks (and
W_{\alpha}
) that appear when the direct theory is
dualized in variant ”b” (not even speaking about their parametrically
different mass scales,
{\overline{\mu}}_{gl,\,\it h}\ll m^{\rm pole}_{\it h}
, see point 1’ in Section 6.1).
This shows the self-contradictory character of duality in variant ”b” =
”confinement without chiral symmetry breaking”.

All other particles in the mass spectrum of the dual theory in this
section 6.2 constitute the sector of lighter particles, with their masses
being parametrically smaller than
{\overline{\mu}}_{gl,\,\it h}
.

2) The next mass scale is formed by a large number of
{\it l}
-flavored dual mesons and
{\rm b}_{\it l}\,,{\rm\overline{b}}_{\it l}

baryons made of non-relativistic (and weakly confined, the string
tension
\sqrt{\sigma}\sim{\Lambda}_{SYM}\ll{\overline{\mu}}^{\rm pole}_{q,\,\it l}
)  dual
\hat{q}^{\it l}\,,\hat{\overline{q}}_{\it l}
quarks with
{\overline{N}}_{c}^{\,\prime}=(N_{\it l}-N_{c})

not higgsed colors. The pole masses of these l-quarks are

{\overline{\mu}}^{\rm pole}_{q,\,\it l}\sim\exp\{3{\overline{N}}_{c}/14\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}\}\,(r)^{(N_{\it l}-N_{c})/N_{c}}\,{\it{\overline{\mu}}_{gl,\,\it h}}/z^{\,\prime}_{q}\ll{\it{\overline{\mu}}_{gl,\,\it h}}

(at
r\ll r_{l}
, see (
6.2.9
) below).

3) Next, there is a large number of gluonia with the mass scale

\sim{\Lambda}_{SYM}\ll{\overline{\mu}}^{\rm pole}_{q,\,\it l}
.

4) Finally, the lightest are
N_{\it l}^{2}
scalar mions
M_{\it ll}

with masses

\mu(M_{\it ll})\sim\exp\{-2{\overline{N}}_{c}/7\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}\}(r)^{2\Delta}\,{\Lambda}_{SYM}/z^{\,\prime}_{M}\ll{\Lambda}_{SYM}\,,\quad 0<\Delta=\frac{3N_{c}-2N_{\it l}}{3N_{c}}<\frac{1}{3}\,.

Comparing the mass spectra of two supposedly equivalent descriptions
in Sections 6.1 and 6.2 above, it is seen that the masses are clearly different
parametrically, in powers
of the parameter
Z_{q}\sim\exp\{-{\overline{N}}_{c}/7\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}\}\ll 1
. Besides, the theory
described in Section 6.2 contains very specific definite numbers of
fields with fixed quantum numbers and spins and with definite masses

\sim{\overline{\mu}}_{gl,\,\it h}\sim Z_{q}^{1/2}m^{\rm pole}_{\it h}\ll m^{\rm pole}_{\it h}
, all
parametrically weakly
interacting
(see point 1 in Section 6.2). No analog of
these distinguished particles is seen in Section 6.1. Instead, there
is a large number of
{\it h}
- flavored hadrons with the masses

\sim m^{\rm pole}_{\it h}
and with various spins, all strongly interacting with
the coupling
a(\mu\sim m^{\rm pole}_{\it h})\sim 1
.

Finally, and we consider this to be of special importance,
there
are no
{\it l}\,,l
-flavored hadrons

H^{\,\prime}_{\it l}

in the theory described in Section 6.2, which have the appropriate
conserved (in the interval of scales
{\overline{\mu}}^{\rm pole}_{q,\,\it l}<\mu<{\overline{\mu}}_{gl,\,\it h}
)
chiral flavors
{\it\overline{l}}\,,{\it l}
and
R
- charges, such that
they can be associated with a large number of various
flavored chiral hadrons made of

{\overline{Q}}^{\it\overline{l}}\,,Q_{\it l}
-quarks (an
W_{\alpha}
), which
are present in Section 5.1 dualized in variant ”b”. This shows that
the duality in the variant ”b” = ”confinement without chiral symmetry
breaking” is not self-consistent. This agrees with some general arguments
presented in
[
11
]
(see Section 7 therein), that the duality in variant
”b”cannot be realized because, in the theory
with unbroken chiral flavor
symmetries and R-charges
and effectively massless quarks, the masses
of flavored and R - charged chiral hadron superfields with various
spins cannot be made ”of nothing”.

This is not the whole story, however. For the mass
{\overline{\mu}}_{gl,\,\it h}
of gluons
in the dual theory to be the largest physical mass

\mu_{H}
as was used in Section 6.2 above, the parameter
r=m_{\it l}/m_{\it h}

has to be taken sufficiently small (see (
6.2.2
),
(
6.2.6
); from now on, the non-leading effects due to
logarithmic factors
like
z^{\,\prime}_{q}\simeq z^{\,\prime\prime}_{q}
are ignored) :

\displaystyle\frac{{\overline{\mu}}^{\rm pole}_{q,\,\it l}}{{\overline{\mu}}_{gl,\,\it h}}\ll 1\,\rightarrow\,r\ll r_{\it l}=\Biggl(\,z^{\,\prime}_{q}\,\exp\Bigl\{-\frac{3{\overline{N}}_{c}}{14\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}}\Bigr\}\,\Biggr)^{\frac{N_{c}}{N_{\it l}-N_{c}}}\sim\exp\Biggl\{-\frac{3{\overline{N}}_{c}}{14\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}}\,\frac{N_{c}}{N_{\it l}-N_{c}}\,\Biggr\}\ll 1\,.

(6.2.9)

We trace below the behavior of the direct and dual theories in the
whole interval
r_{\it l}<r<1
.

9
9

9

For this, it is convenient to keep
m_{\it h}
intact while
m_{\it l}
will be
decreased, starting from
m_{\it l}=m_{\it h}
.

As regards the direct theory (see Section 6.1 above), its regime and
all hierarchies in the mass spectrum remain the same for any value of

r<1
, i.e. the pole mass
m^{\rm pole}_{\it h}
of the
Q^{\it h}\,,{\overline{Q}}_{\it h}
quarks becomes
the largest physical mass
\mu_{H}
already at
r<1/(\rm several)
, and
so on.

But this is not the case for the dual theory (see Section 6.2 above).
At
r_{\it l}<r<1/(\rm several)
, the pole mass
{\hat{\mu}}^{\rm pole}_{q,\,\it l}
of
{q}_{\it l}\,,{\overline{q}}^{\it l}

quarks remains the largest physical mass

\displaystyle\frac{{{\hat{\mu}}^{\rm pole}_{q,\,\it l}}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\sim\frac{\langle M_{\it ll}\rangle}{Z_{q}\Lambda_{Q}^{2}}\,\Biggl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{{\hat{\mu}}^{\rm pole}_{q,\,\it l}}\Biggr)^{\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}/N_{F}}\sim\exp\Biggl\{\,\frac{{\overline{N}}_{c}}{7\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}}\,\Biggr\}\,\Bigl(r\Bigr)^{\Bigl(\frac{N_{\it l}-N_{c}}{N_{c}}\frac{N_{F}}{3{\overline{N}}_{c}}\Bigr)}\,\Biggl(\frac{m_{\it h}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Biggr)^{N_{F}/3N_{c}}\sim\frac{{\overline{\mu}}^{\rm pole}_{q,\,\it l}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\,.

(6.2.10)

Already this is sufficient to see a qualitative difference between
the direct and dual theories. The
hh
-flavored hadrons in the
direct theory have the largest masses, while the
ll
- flavored
hadrons are the heaviest ones in the dual theory.

We make now some rough additional estimates in Section 6.2 at

r>r_{\it l}
. After integrating out the heaviest quarks
{q}_{\it l}\,,{\overline{q}}^{\it l}

at the scale
\mu\sim{\hat{\mu}}^{\rm pole}_{q,\,\it l}
, the dual theory remains
with
{\overline{N}}_{c}
colors and
N_{\it h}<{\overline{N}}_{c}
dual quarks
{\overline{q}}_{\it\overline{h}}\,,\,q^{\it h}
(and mions
M
), and with
\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}^{\prime\prime}=(3{\overline{N}}_{c}-N_{\it h})>0
. It is in the weak-coupling logarithmic regime
at
\mu^{\,\prime}_{H}<\mu<{\hat{\mu}}^{\rm pole}_{q,\,\it l}
,    where
\mu^{\,\prime}_{H}
is
the highest mass scale in the remaining theory. The new scale factor

\Lambda^{\,\prime}_{q}
of its gauge coupling can be found from

\displaystyle\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}^{\,\prime\prime}\ln\Biggl(\frac{{\hat{\mu}}^{\rm pole}_{q,\,\it l}}{\Lambda^{\,\prime}_{q}}\Biggr)\simeq\frac{2\pi}{\overline{\alpha}_{*}}=\frac{3{\overline{N}}_{c}^{2}}{7\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}}\quad\rightarrow\quad\frac{\Lambda^{\,\prime}_{q}}{{\hat{\mu}}^{\rm pole}_{q,\,\it l}}\sim\exp\Biggl\{-\frac{3{\overline{N}}_{c}^{2}}{7\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}^{\,\prime\prime}}\Biggr\}\ll 1\,.

(6.2.11)

If (see below)
r_{\it l}\ll r_{\it h}<r<1/(\rm several)
, then

\mu^{\,\prime}_{H}={\hat{\mu}}^{\rm pole}_{q,\,\it h}>\Lambda^{\,\prime}_{q}
, where
{\hat{\mu}}^{\rm pole}_{q,\,\it h}

is the pole mass of
q_{\it h}\,,{\overline{q}}^{\it h}
quarks which are in the HQ-phase
(i.e. not yet higgsed ). Roughly,
{\hat{\mu}}^{\rm pole}_{q,\,\it h}\sim r\,{\hat{\mu}}^{\rm pole}_{q,\,\it l}
, hence

\displaystyle\frac{{\hat{\mu}}^{\rm pole}_{q,\,\it h}}{\Lambda^{\,\prime}_{q}}>1\quad\rightarrow\quad r>r_{\it h}\sim\exp\Biggl\{-\frac{3{\overline{N}}_{c}^{2}}{7\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}^{\,\prime\prime}}\Biggr\}\gg r_{\it l}\,.

(6.2.12)

In the interval
r_{\it h}<r<1/(\rm several)
, the mass spectrum of
the dual theory is qualitatively
not much different from the case
r=1
(see Section 4). The heaviest
are the
ll
- hadrons,
then the
hl
- hadrons, then the
hh
- hadrons, then
gluonia and, in addition, there are the
mions
M_{\it hh}\,,\,M_{\it hl}\,,\,M_{\it ll}
with their masses

\mu(M_{\it hh})\sim m_{\it h}^{2}/\mu_{o}\,,\,\,\,\mu(M_{\it hl})\sim m_{\it h}m_{\it l}/\mu_{o}\,,\,\,\,\mu(M_{\it ll})\sim m_{\it l}^{2}/\mu_{o}\,,

where

\mu_{o}=z_{M}(\Lambda_{Q},{\hat{\mu}}^{\rm pole}_{q,\,\it l}){\Lambda}_{SYM}^{3}/Z_{q}^{2}\Lambda_{Q}^{2}\,,\quad z_{M}(\Lambda_{Q},{\hat{\mu}}^{\rm pole}_{q,\,\it l})\sim(\Lambda_{Q}^{2}/m_{\it h}m_{\it l})^{\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}/3N_{c}}\gg 1.

As
r
decreases further, a phase transition occurs from the
\rm HQ_{\it h}
phase to the
\rm Higgs_{\it h}
phase at

r\sim r_{\it h}
, i.e. after
{\hat{\mu}}^{\rm pole}_{q,\,\it h}>\Lambda^{\prime}_{q}

becomes
{\hat{\mu}}^{\rm pole}_{q,\,\it h}<\Lambda^{\,\prime}_{q}
and the quarks
q_{\it h}\,,{\overline{q}}^{\it h}
are
higgsed. But even then the
{q}_{\it l}\,,{\overline{q}}^{\it l}
quarks remain the heaviest
ones. And only when
r
becomes
r<r_{\it l}\ll r_{\it h}\ll 1
, the
gluon mass
{\overline{\mu}}_{gl,\,\it h}
becomes the largest and the
mass spectrum of the dual theory becomes that described above in this
section 6.2.

7

Direct theory. Unequal quark masses,
\mathbf{3N_{c}/2<N_{F}<3N_{c}},\\
\mathbf{(3N_{c}-N_{F})/N_{F}={\cal O}(1)\,,\,\,N_{c}<N_{\it l}<3N_{c}/2}

Let us check first that largest observable masses have heavy quarks

Q^{\it h}\,,{\overline{Q}}_{\it h}
. The pole mass of
Q^{\it h}
will be in this case

\displaystyle m^{\rm pole}_{\it h}=\frac{m_{h}}{z_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q},m^{\rm pole}_{\it h})},\,\,z_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q},m^{\rm pole}_{\it h})\sim\bigl(\frac{m^{\rm pole}_{\it h}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\bigr)^{\gamma_{Q}}\rightarrow\frac{m^{\rm pole}_{\it h}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\sim\bigl(\frac{m_{\it h}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\bigr)^{\frac{N_{F}}{3N_{c}}},\,\,\gamma_{Q}=\frac{3N_{c}-N_{F}}{N_{F}},\,\,\quad

(7.0.1)

The competing mass of the gluon due to possible higgsing of
Q^{\it l}

quarks (if it were the largest one) would be as follows.

\mu_{gl,l}^{2}\sim z_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu_{gl,l})\langle M_{l}\rangle,\quad z_{Q}\sim(\mu_{gl,l}/\Lambda_{Q})^{\rm{b_{o}}/N_{F}},\,\,\frac{\langle M_{l}\rangle}{\Lambda_{Q}^{2}}\sim\bigl(r\bigr)^{\frac{N_{l}-N_{c}}{N_{c}}}\Bigl(\frac{Q^{\it h}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\frac{N_{F}-N_{c}}{N_{c}}},\,\,r=\frac{m_{l}}{m_{h}}\ll 1\,,

\displaystyle\frac{\mu_{gl,l}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\sim\Bigl(\frac{\langle M_{l}\rangle}{\Lambda_{Q}^{2}}\Bigr)^{N_{F}/3{\overline{N}}_{c}}\rightarrow\frac{\mu_{gl,l}}{m^{\rm pole}_{\it h}}\sim\bigl(r\bigr)^{\frac{N_{l}-N_{c}}{N_{c}}}\ll 1\,.

(7.0.2)

Therefore,
m^{\rm pole}_{\it h}
is the largest mass. After
Q^{\it h},{\overline{Q^{\it h}}}
have
been integrated out in the conformal regime at
m^{\rm pole}_{\it h}<\mu<\Lambda_{Q}
,
all particle masses in the lower energy theory with
N_{c}
colors and

N_{c}<N_{\it l}<3N_{c}/2
lighter
Q^{\it l}\,,{\overline{Q}}_{\it l}
quarks are parametrically
smaller than the scale
\Lambda_{Q}^{\,\prime}\sim m^{\rm pole}_{\it h}
(see (
6.1.1
)
above), and at
\mu<\Lambda_{Q}^{\,\prime}
this lower energy theory enters
the strong coupling regime with
a(\mu\ll\Lambda_{Q}^{\,\prime})\gg 1
.

10
10

10

Hereafter we use the anomalous quark
dimension
1+\gamma_{Q}=N_{c}/(N_{F}-N_{c})
and the strong coupling

a(\mu)\gg 1
, see section 5 for explanations.

This lower-energy theory is in the HQ-phase (see footnote

5
) with
N_{l}>N_{c}
flavors this time, and the pole mass
of
Q^{\it l}\,,{\overline{Q}}_{\it l}
quarks is

\frac{m^{\rm pole}_{\it l}}{\Lambda_{Q}^{\,\prime}}\sim\Biggl(\frac{m_{\it l}(\mu=\Lambda_{Q}^{\,\prime})}{\Lambda_{Q}^{\,\prime}}=r\,\Biggr)\Biggl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}^{\,\prime}}{m^{\rm pole}_{\it l}}\Biggr)^{\gamma^{\,\prime}_{Q}}\quad\rightarrow\quad\frac{m^{\rm pole}_{\it l}}{\Lambda_{Q}^{\,\prime}}\sim\Bigl(r\Bigr)^{(N_{\it l}-N_{c})/N_{c}}\ll 1\,,\quad r\ll 1\,,

\displaystyle{\rm b}^{\,\prime}_{o}=3N_{c}-N_{\it l},\quad 1+\gamma^{\,\prime}_{Q}=\frac{N_{c}}{N_{\it l}-N_{c}}\,,\quad\nu=\frac{N_{F}\gamma_{Q}^{\,\prime}-{\rm b}^{\,\prime}_{\rm o}}{N_{c}}=\frac{3N_{c}-2N_{\it l}}{N_{\it l}-N_{c}}\,.

(7.0.3)

The coupling
a_{+}(\mu=m^{\rm pole}_{\it l})
is large

\displaystyle a_{+}(\mu=m^{\rm pole}_{\it l})\sim\Biggl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}^{\prime}}{\mu=m^{\rm pole}_{\it l}}\Biggr)^{\nu}\sim\Bigl(\,\frac{1}{r}\,\Bigr)^{(3N_{c}-2N_{\it l})/N_{c}}\gg 1\,.

(7.0.4)

Hence, after integrating out all
Q^{\it l}\,,{\overline{Q}}_{\it l}
quarks as heavy ones at

\mu<m^{\rm pole}_{\it l}
, we are left with the pure
SU(N_{c})
SYM theory, but it
is now in the
strong coupling
branch
. This is somewhat unusual, but there is no contradiction
because this perturbative strong coupling regime with

a_{SYM}(\mu)\gg 1
is realized in a restricted interval of scales
only,
{\Lambda}_{SYM}\ll\mu<m^{\rm pole}_{\it l}\ll\Lambda_{Q}^{\prime}
. It follows from the NSVZ
\beta

function
[
12
]
that the coupling
a_{SYM}(\mu\gg{\Lambda}_{SYM})\gg 1
is
then given by

a_{SYM}(\mu=m^{\rm pole}_{\it l})=\Biggl(\frac{\mu=m^{\rm pole}_{\it l}}{\lambda_{SYM}}\Biggr)^{3}=a_{+}(\mu=m^{\rm pole}_{\it l})\,\rightarrow

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda_{SYM}}{\Lambda_{Q}}=\frac{{\Lambda}_{SYM}}{\Lambda_{Q}}=\Biggl[\Bigl(\frac{m_{\it l}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{N_{\it l}}\Bigl(\frac{m_{\it h}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{N_{\it h}}\Biggr]^{\frac{1}{3N_{c}}},

(7.0.5)

\displaystyle\frac{{\Lambda}_{SYM}}{m^{\rm pole}_{\it l}}=r^{\frac{3N_{c}-2N_{\it l}}{3N_{c}}}\ll 1,\quad a_{SYM}({\Lambda}_{SYM}\ll\mu<m^{\rm pole}_{\it l})=\Bigl(\frac{\mu}{{\Lambda}_{SYM}}\Bigr)^{3}\,,

(7.0.6)

and it now
decreases
from
a_{SYM}(\mu=m^{\rm pole}_{\it l})\gg 1
to

a_{SYM}(\mu\sim{\Lambda}_{SYM})\sim 1
as
\mu
decreases, after which the
non-perturbative effects of pure SYM stop the perturbative
RG-evolution at
\mu\sim{\Lambda}_{SYM}
.

Therefore, decreasing the scale
\mu
from
\mu=m^{\rm pole}_{\it l}
to
\mu<{\Lambda}_{SYM}
,
integrating
out all gauge degrees of freedom except for the one whole field
S\sim W_{\alpha}^{2}
, and using the VY-form of the superpotential of the
field
S

[
15
]
, we obtain the universal value of
{\Lambda}_{SYM}
and the
standard gluino condensate,
\langle S\rangle={\Lambda}_{SYM}^{3}
.

To verify the self-consistency, we have to estimate the scale

\mu_{\rm gl}
of
the possible higgsing of
Q^{\it l}\,,{\overline{Q}}_{\it l}
quarks. This estimate looks as,
see (
7.0.3
)

\displaystyle\mu_{\rm gl}^{2}\sim a_{+}(\mu=\mu_{\rm gl})\langle{\overline{Q}}_{\it l}Q^{\it l}\rangle_{\mu=\mu_{\rm gl}}\sim\Bigl(m^{\rm pole}_{\it l}\Bigr)^{2}\,,\quad a_{+}(\mu=\mu_{\rm gl})\sim\Biggl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}^{\,\prime}\sim m^{\rm pole}_{\it h}}{\mu_{\rm gl}}\Biggr)^{\nu}\,,

(7.0.7)

\langle{\overline{Q}}_{\it l}Q^{\it l}\rangle_{\mu=\mu_{\rm gl}}\sim\langle{\overline{Q}}_{\it l}Q^{\it l}\rangle_{\mu=\Lambda_{Q}}\,\Biggl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}^{\,\prime}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Biggr)^{\gamma_{Q}=(\rm{b_{o}}/N_{F})}\,\Biggl(\frac{\mu_{\rm gl}}{\Lambda_{Q}^{\,\prime}}\Biggr)^{\gamma^{\,\prime}_{Q}}\,,\quad\langle{\overline{Q}}_{\it l}Q^{\it l}\rangle_{\mu=\Lambda_{Q}}=\frac{{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{3}}{m_{\it l}}\,.

As before, the Konishi anomaly shows that
Q^{\it l}\,,{\overline{Q}}_{\it l}
quarks are not
higgsed at
N_{l}>N_{c}
and the
HQ_{\it l}
phase is self-consistent

5
.

On the whole, all quarks of the direct theory are in the HQ phase
(and are therefore confined, but the string tension is small in
comparison with quark masses,
\sqrt{\sigma}\sim{\Lambda}_{SYM}\ll m^{\rm pole}_{\it l}\ll m^{\rm pole}_{\it h}
. This is “the weak confinement” .
The mass spectrum then includes  :  1)  a large number of heavy

\it hh
-flavored mesons with the mass scale

\sim m^{\rm pole}_{\it h}\ll\Lambda_{Q}
;   2)  a large number of hybrid
\it hl
-mesons and baryons
B_{\it hl}\,,{\overline{B}}_{\it hl}
with the same mass scale
\sim m^{\rm pole}_{\it h}
;
3)  a large number
of
\it ll
-flavored mesons and baryons
B_{\it l}\,,{\overline{B}}_{\it l}
with the mass
scale
\sim m^{\rm pole}_{\it l}\ll m^{\rm pole}_{\it h}
, and finally,   4)  gluonia, which are
the lightest, with their mass scale
\sim{\Lambda}_{SYM}\ll m^{\rm pole}_{\it l}
.

8

Direct and dual theories. Unequal quark masses.

N_{c}<N_{F}<3N_{c}/2,\,\,N_{\it l}>N_{c}

As regards the direct theory, the mass spectrum in this case with

r=m_{\it l}/m_{\it h}\ll 1
is not much different from the one in the preceding
section. All quarks are in the (very) strongly coupled HQ phase and
are not higgsed but confined with
a(\mu\ll\Lambda_{Q})\gg 1
, and the
highest physical mass is the pole mass of
h
- quarks

\displaystyle m^{\rm pole}_{\it h}=m_{\it h}\Biggl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{m^{\rm pole}_{\it h}}\Biggr)^{\gamma_{+}}\quad\rightarrow\quad\frac{m^{\rm pole}_{\it h}}{\Lambda_{Q}}=\Biggl(\frac{m_{\it h}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Biggr)^{(N_{F}-N_{c})/N_{c}}\,,\quad\gamma_{+}=\frac{(2N_{c}-N_{F})}{(N_{F}-N_{c})}\,.

(8.0.1)

After integrating out
Q^{\it h}\,,{\overline{Q}}_{\it h}
quarks as heavy ones at

\mu<m^{\rm pole}_{\it h}
, the lower energy theory remains with
N_{c}
colors
and
N_{c}<N_{\it l}<3N_{c}/2

l
- quarks,
in the strong coupling regime with
a(m^{\rm pole}_{\it l}<\mu<m^{\rm pole}_{\it h})\gg 1
, see
sections 5, 6, 7. The next independent physical scale is the pole mass
of
l
- quarks,
\gamma_{-}=(2N_{c}-N_{\it l})/(N_{\it l}-N_{c})
,

\displaystyle m^{\rm pole}_{\it l}\sim\Biggl(m_{\it l}(\mu=m^{\rm pole}_{\it h})=r\,m^{\rm pole}_{\it h}\Biggr)\,\Biggl(\frac{m^{\rm pole}_{\it h}}{m^{\rm pole}_{\it l}}\Biggr)^{\gamma_{-}}\quad\rightarrow\quad\Bigl(\frac{m^{\rm pole}_{\it l}}{m^{\rm pole}_{\it h}}\Bigr)\sim\Bigl(r\Bigr)^{\frac{N_{\it l}-N_{c}}{N_{c}}}\ll 1\,.

(8.0.2)

After integrating out the
l
- quarks as heavy ones at

\mu<m^{\rm pole}_{\it l}
, leaves us with the
SU(N_{c})
SYM theory in the
strong-coupling branch with the large gauge
coupling. The scale factor
{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{\,\prime}
of its gauge
coupling is determined from the matching (see sections 6, 7)

\displaystyle\Biggl(\frac{m^{\rm pole}_{\it l}}{{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{\,\prime}}\Biggr)^{3}=\Biggl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{m^{\rm pole}_{\it h}}\Biggr)^{\nu_{+}}\Biggl(\frac{m^{\rm pole}_{\it h}}{m^{\rm pole}_{\it l}}\Biggr)^{\nu_{-}}\quad\rightarrow\quad{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{\,\prime}={\Lambda}_{SYM}=\Biggl(\Lambda_{Q}^{\rm{b_{o}}}m_{\it l}^{N_{\it l}}m_{\it h}^{N_{\it h}}\Biggr)^{1/3N_{c}}\,.

(8.0.3)

\nu_{+}=\frac{3N_{c}-2N_{F}}{N_{F}-N_{c}}\,,\quad\nu_{-}=\frac{3N_{c}-2N_{\it l}}{N_{\it l}-N_{c}}>\nu_{+}\,\,.

To verify the self-consistency, we also estimate the gluon masses due
to possible higgsing of the
Q^{\it h}
and/or
Q^{\it l}
- quarks. As for

Q^{\it h}
- quarks ,

\displaystyle\hskip-8.53581pt\frac{\mu_{\it gl,\,h}^{2}}{\Lambda_{Q}^{2}}\sim\Biggl[\,a_{+}(\mu=\mu_{\it gl,\,h})=\Biggl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{\mu_{\it gl,\,h}}\Biggr)^{\nu_{+}}\,\Biggr]\,\frac{\langle{\overline{Q}}^{\it h}Q^{\it h}\rangle}{\Lambda_{Q}^{2}}\,\Biggl(\frac{\mu_{\it gl,\,h}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Biggr)^{\gamma_{+}}\,\rightarrow\,\,\frac{\mu_{\it gl,\,h}}{m^{\rm pole}_{\it h}}\sim\Bigl(\,r\,\Bigr)^{N_{\it l}/N_{c}}\ll 1\,,

(8.0.4)

and hence there is no problem, but for
Q^{\it l}
- quarks, we now
have

\displaystyle\frac{\mu_{{\rm gl},\,L}^{2}}{\Lambda_{Q}^{2}}\sim\Biggl[\,a_{-}(\mu=\mu_{{\rm gl},\,L})=\Biggl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{m^{\rm pole}_{\it h}}\Biggr)^{\nu_{+}}\Biggl(\frac{m^{\rm pole}_{\it h}}{\mu_{{\rm gl},\,L}}\Biggr)^{\nu_{-}}\,\Biggr]\,\frac{\langle{\overline{Q}}^{\it l}Q^{\it l}\rangle}{\Lambda_{Q}^{2}}\,\Biggl(\frac{m^{\rm pole}_{\it h}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Biggr)^{\gamma_{+}}\Biggl(\frac{\mu_{{\rm gl},\,L}}{m^{\rm pole}_{\it h}}\Biggr)^{\gamma_{-}}\,

(8.0.5)

\displaystyle\rightarrow\quad\frac{\mu_{{\rm gl},\,L}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\sim\frac{\langle{\overline{Q}}^{\it l}Q^{\it l}\rangle}{\Lambda_{Q}^{2}}\,\sim\,\frac{m^{\rm pole}_{\it l}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\sim\Bigl(r\Bigr)^{\frac{N_{\it l}-N_{c}}{N_{c}}}\,\,\frac{m^{\rm pole}_{\it h}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\,.

(8.0.6)

As before, because
N_{l}>N_{c}
, it is seen from the Konishi anomaly
(
2.1.2
) that l-qurks are not higgsed due to the rank restrictions,
see footnote
5
.

On the whole in the direct theory, all quarks are in the HQ phase and
the mass spectrum consists of :  a)  a large number of
hh
and hybrid

hl
- mesons and baryons with the mass scale
\sim m^{\rm pole}_{\it h}\sim\Lambda_{Q}\,(m_{\it h}/\Lambda_{Q})^{(N_{F}-N_{c})/N_{c}}\ll\Lambda_{Q}
,   b)  a
large number of
ll
- mesons and baryons with the mass scale

\sim m^{\rm pole}_{\it l}\sim(r)^{(N_{\it l}-N_{c})/N_{c}}\,m^{\rm pole}_{\it h}\ll m^{\rm pole}_{\it h}
;  all quarks are
weakly confined, i.e. the string tension
\sqrt{\sigma}\sim{\Lambda}_{SYM}
is
much smaller than their masses ,   c)  gluonia, which are the
lightest, with the masses
\sim{\Lambda}_{SYM}\ll m^{\rm pole}_{\it l}
.

In the IR-free dual theory there are
several regimes depending on the value of
r=m_{\it l}/m_{\it h}<1
.

i) At
r_{1}=(m_{\it h}/\Lambda_{Q})^{(3N_{c}-2N_{F})/2N_{\it l}}<r<1
,  the hierarchy of
masses looks
as :
\mu_{q,\,\it l}>\mu_{q,\,\it h}>\mu_{\rm gl}^{\it h}
, where

\mu_{q,\,\it l}
and

\mu_{q,\,\it h}
are the pole masses of dual quarks and
\mu_{\rm gl}^{\it h}

is the gluon mass due to possible higgsing of
q_{\it h},\,{\overline{q}}_{\it h}
quarks (all logarithmic renormalization effects are
ignored here and below in this section for simplicity):

\frac{\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,\,\it h}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\sim\Bigl(r\Bigr)^{\frac{N_{l}}{N_{c}}}\Bigl(\frac{m_{h}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\frac{N_{F}-N_{c}}{N_{c}}}\,.

\displaystyle\frac{\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,\,\it l}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\sim\Biggl(\frac{\langle{\overline{Q}}_{\it l}Q_{\it l}(\mu=\Lambda_{Q})\rangle}{\Lambda_{Q}^{2}}\Biggr)^{\frac{1}{1+\gamma_{+}}}\sim\Bigl(r\Bigr)^{\frac{N_{\it l}-N_{c}}{N_{c}}}\,\Bigl(\frac{m^{\rm pole}_{\it h}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\frac{N_{F}-N_{c}}{N_{c}}},

(8.0.7)

\displaystyle\frac{\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,\,\it h}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\sim\frac{\mu_{q,\,\it h}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\sim\frac{\langle{\overline{Q}}_{\it h}Q_{\it h}(\mu=\Lambda_{Q})\rangle}{\Lambda_{Q}^{2}}\sim\Bigl(r\Bigr)^{\frac{N_{\it l}}{N_{c}}}\,\Bigl(\frac{m_{\it h}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\frac{N_{F}-N_{c}}{N_{c}}}\,<\,\frac{\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,\,\it l}}{\Lambda_{Q}},

(8.0.8)

\mu_{\rm gl}^{\it h}\sim|\langle{\overline{q}}_{\it h}q_{\it h}\rangle|^{1/2}\sim(m_{\it h}\Lambda_{Q})^{1/2}\,<\,\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,\,\it h}\,.

This hierarchy shows that all dual quarks are in the HQ phase and not
higgsed but confined by the dual
SU({\overline{N}}_{c}>1)
SYM..

ii) At
r_{2}=(m_{\it h}/\Lambda_{Q})^{(3N_{c}-2N_{F})/2(N_{\it l}-N_{c})}<r<r_{1}=(m_{\it h}/\Lambda_{Q})^{(3N_{c}-2N_{F})/2N_{\it l}}
,
the hierarchy of masses looks as :
\mu_{q,\,\it l}>\mu_{\rm gl}^{\it h}>\mu_{q,\,\it h}
. This shows that
{\overline{q}}_{\it l},\,q_{\it l}

quarks are in the HQ phase and are the heaviest ones, while
{\overline{q}}_{\it h},\,q_{\it h}
quarks are higgsed. The phase transition of
{\overline{q}}_{\it h},\,q_{\it h}
quarks from the
{\rm HQ}_{\it h}
phase to the
{\rm Higgs}_{\it h}
phase occurs in the region
r\sim r_{1}
.

iii) At
r<r_{2}=(m_{\it h}/\Lambda_{Q})^{(3N_{c}-2N_{F})/2(N_{\it l}-N_{c})}
,  the hierarchy
of masses looks as :
\mu_{\rm gl}^{\it h}>\mu_{q,\,\it l}>\mu_{q,\,\it h}
.
Here, the
{\overline{q}}_{\it h},\,q_{\it h}
quarks are higgsed and

\mu_{\rm gl}^{\it h}
is the highest mass, while the
{\overline{q}}_{\it l},\,q_{\it l}
quarks are lighter and are in the HQ phase. We now give some
details.

i) After the heaviest dual
\it l
- quarks are integrated out at

\mu<\mu_{q,\,\it l}
, a theory remains with
{\overline{N}}_{c}
colors and

N_{\it h}<{\overline{N}}_{c}
quarks
{\overline{q}}_{\it h},\,q_{\it h}
(and mions
M
), and
with the scale factor of its gauge coupling

\displaystyle\Bigl(\Lambda^{\,\prime}_{q}\Bigr)^{{\overline{b}}^{\,\prime}_{o}}=\Lambda_{Q}^{\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}}\,\mu^{N_{\it l}}_{q,\,\it l}\,,\quad{\overline{b}}^{\,\prime}_{o}=(3{\overline{N}}_{c}-N_{\it h})>0\,,\quad\Biggl(\frac{\Lambda^{\,\prime}_{q}}{\mu_{q,\,\it h}}\Biggr)^{{\overline{b}}^{\,\prime}_{o}/{\overline{N}}_{c}}=\Bigl(\frac{r_{1}}{r}\Bigr)^{2N_{\it l}/N_{c}}<1\,.

(8.0.9)

Hence, after integrating out the quarks
{\overline{q}}_{\it h},\,q_{\it h}
as heavy ones with masses
\mu_{q,\,\it h}>\Lambda^{\,\prime}_{q}
and then gluons via the
VY - procedure, we obtain the Lagrangian of mions

\displaystyle\overline{K}=\frac{1}{\Lambda_{Q}^{2}}\,{\rm Tr}\,\Bigl(M^{\dagger}M\Bigr)\,,\quad{\overline{{\mathcal{W}}}}=-{\overline{N}}_{c}\,\Biggl(\frac{\rm\det\,M}{\Lambda_{Q}^{\rm{b_{o}}}}\Biggr)^{1/{\overline{N}}_{c}}+{\rm Tr}\,(m_{Q}M)\,\,.

(8.0.10)

It describes mions
{\rm M}
with the masses

\displaystyle\mu(M^{i}_{j})\sim\frac{m_{i}m_{j}\Lambda_{Q}^{2}}{{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{3}}<{\Lambda}_{SYM}\,\,,\quad i,\,j={\it l\,,\,h}\,.

(8.0.11)

ii) The first step of integrating out the heaviest
\it l
- quarks
with masses

\mu_{q,\,\it l}
is the same. But now, at
r_{2}<r<r_{1}
, the next
physical mass is
{\overline{\mu}}_{gl,\,\it h}>\Lambda^{\,\prime}_{q}

due to higgsing of dual
\it h
- quarks, with
{\overline{N}}_{c}\rightarrow({\overline{N}}_{c}-N_{\it h})
and
formation of
N_{\it h}^{2}
nions
N_{\it hh}
. After integrating out
the heavy higgsed gluons
and their superpartners with masses
{\overline{\mu}}_{gl,\,\it h}\sim(m_{\it h}\Lambda_{Q})^{1/2}
, and
not higgsed gluons via the VY - procedure,
the Lagrangian of the remaining degrees of freedom takes the form

{\overline{K}}=\frac{{\rm Tr}\,\Bigl(M^{\dagger}M\Bigr)}{\Lambda_{Q}^{2}}+2\,{\rm Tr}\sqrt{N^{\dagger}_{\it hh}N_{\it hh}}\,,\quad{\overline{{\mathcal{W}}}}=-(N_{\it l}-N_{c})\,\Biggl(\frac{\det{M_{\it ll}}}{\Lambda_{Q}^{\rm{b_{o}}}\,{\det\Bigl(-N_{\it hh}/\Lambda_{Q}\Bigr)}}\Biggr)^{1/(N_{\it l}-N_{c})}+

\displaystyle+\,\frac{1}{\Lambda_{Q}}{\rm Tr}\,N_{\it hh}\Biggl(M_{\it hh}-M_{\it hl}M^{-1}_{\it ll}M_{\it lh}\Biggr)+{\rm Tr}\Bigl(m_{\it l}M_{\it ll}+m_{\it h}M_{\it hh}\Bigr)\,.

(8.0.12)

From this, the masses of mions
M_{\it hh},\,M_{\it ll}
,  hybrids

M_{\it lh},\,M_{\it hl}
, and nions
N_{\it hh}
are

\displaystyle\mu(M_{\it hh})\sim\mu(N_{\it hh})\sim\Bigl(m_{\it h}\Lambda_{Q}\Bigr)^{1/2}\sim{\overline{\mu}}_{gl,\,\it h}\,,

(8.0.13)

\mu(M_{\it lh})\sim\mu(M_{\it hl})\sim\frac{m_{\it l}m_{\it h}\Lambda_{Q}^{2}}{{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{3}}\,,\quad\mu(M_{\it ll})\sim\frac{m_{\it l}^{2}\Lambda_{Q}^{2}}{{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{3}}\,.

iii) The heaviest particles in this region
r<r_{2}
are higgsed gluons
and their
superpartners with masses
{\overline{\mu}}_{gl,\,\it h}\sim(m_{\it h}\Lambda_{Q})^{1/2}
. After these
have been integrated out, there remain

{\overline{N}}_{c}^{\,\prime}=(N_{\it l}-N_{c})
dual colors and
N_{\it l}
flavors with active
not higgsed colors, and the regime at
\mu<{\overline{\mu}}_{gl,\,\it h}
is IR - free logarithmic,

\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}^{\,\prime}=(3{\overline{N}}_{c}^{\,\prime}-N_{\it l})=(2N_{\it l}-3N_{c})<0
. In this theory,
the next independent physical scale is given by the mass
\mu_{q,\,\it l}

of the
N_{\it l}
active
\it l
- quarks with unhiggsed colors.

The masses of
2N_{\it l}N_{\it h}
hybrid mions
M_{\it hl}+M_{\it lh}
and

2N_{\it l}N_{\it h}
nions

N_{\it lh}+N_{\it hl}
(these are the dual
{\it l}
-quarks that
have higgsed colors) are determined mainly
by their common mass term in the superpotential :

\displaystyle K_{\rm hybr}\simeq{\rm Tr}\,\Biggl(\frac{M^{\dagger}_{\it hl}M_{\it hl}}{\Lambda_{Q}^{2}}\Biggr)+{\rm Tr}\,\Bigl(N^{\dagger}_{\it lh}N_{\it lh}\Bigr)\,,\quad{\mathcal{W}}=\Bigl(m_{\it h}\Lambda_{Q}\Bigr)^{1/2}{\rm Tr}\,\Biggl(\frac{M_{\it hl}N_{\it lh}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Biggr)\,,

(8.0.14)

and hence their masses are

\displaystyle\mu(M_{\it hl})\sim\mu(M_{\it lh})\sim\mu(N_{\it hl})\sim\mu(N_{\it lh})\sim{\overline{\mu}}_{gl,\,\it h}\,.

(8.0.15)

Passing to lower scales and integrating out first active
\it l

-quarks as heavy ones with masses
\mu_{q,\,\it l}
and then
not higgsed gluons via the VY-procedure, we obtain

\displaystyle K=\frac{{\rm Tr}\,\Bigl(M^{\dagger}_{\it ll}M_{\it ll}+M^{\dagger}_{\it hh}M_{\it hh}\Bigr)}{\Lambda_{Q}^{2}}+2\,{\rm Tr}\sqrt{N^{\dagger}_{\it hh}N_{\it hh}}\,,

(8.0.16)

{\overline{{\mathcal{W}}}}=-(N_{\it l}-N_{c})\,\Biggl(\frac{\det{M_{\it ll}}}{\Lambda_{Q}^{\rm{b_{o}}}\,{\det\Bigl(-N_{\it hh}/\Lambda_{Q}\Bigr)}}\Biggr)^{1/(N_{\it l}-N_{c})}+\,\frac{{\rm Tr}\,(N_{\it hh}M_{\it hh})}{\Lambda_{Q}}+{\rm Tr}\Bigl(m_{\it l}M_{\it ll}+m_{\it h}M_{\it hh}\Bigr)\,.

From (
8.0.16
), finally, the masses are given by

\displaystyle\mu(N_{\it hh})\sim\mu(M_{\it hh})\sim{\overline{\mu}}_{gl,\,\it h}\sim\Bigl(m_{\it h}\Lambda_{Q}\Bigr)^{1/2}\,,\quad\mu(M_{\it ll})\sim\frac{m_{\it l}^{2}\Lambda_{Q}^{2}}{{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{3}}\,.

(8.0.17)

9

Direct theory. Unequal quark masses.

N_{c}<N_{F}<3N_{c}/2\,,\,\,N_{\it l}<N_{c}-1

In this regime, at
r=m_{\it l}/m_{\it h}\ll 1
, the highest physical scale

\mu_{H}
is determined by
the gluon masses
\mu_{{\rm gl},\,L}
that arise due to higgsing of the

Q^{\it l}\,,{\overline{Q}}_{\it l}
- quarks :

11
11

11

Here and below, the value of

r
is taken to be not too small,  such that

\mu_{{\rm gl},\,L}\ll\Lambda_{Q}
, i.e.
r_{H}\ll r\ll 1,\,r_{H}=(m_{\it h}/\Lambda_{Q})^{\rho},\,\rho={\overline{N}}_{c}/(N_{c}-N_{\it l})
. At
r
so small that
r\ll r_{H},\,\mu_{{\rm gl},\,L}\gg\Lambda_{Q}
, the quarks
Q^{\it l}\,,{\overline{Q}}_{\it l}
are higgsed in the logarithmic
weak coupling region and the form of the RG flow is different, but
the regime is qualitatively the same for
\mu_{{\rm gl},\,L}\ll\Lambda_{Q}
or

\mu_{{\rm gl},\,L}\gg\Lambda_{Q}
, and nothing happens as
\mu_{{\rm gl},\,L}
overshoots
\Lambda_{Q}
in the
scenario considered here.
Besides, l-quarks are higgsed only at not too large values of
N_{c}
,
see
[
18
]
.

\frac{\mu_{{\rm gl},\,L}^{2}}{\Lambda_{Q}^{2}}\sim\Biggl[\,a_{+}(\mu=\mu_{{\rm gl},\,L})=\Biggl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{\mu_{{\rm gl},\,L}}\Biggr)^{\nu_{+}}\,\Biggr]\,\frac{\langle{\overline{Q}}_{\it l}Q^{\it l}\rangle}{\Lambda_{Q}^{2}}\,\Biggl(\frac{\mu_{{\rm gl},\,L}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Biggr)^{\gamma_{+}}\,\quad\rightarrow

\displaystyle\frac{\mu_{{\rm gl},\,L}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\sim\frac{\langle{\overline{Q}}^{\it l}Q_{\it l}\rangle}{\Lambda_{Q}^{2}}\sim\frac{{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{3}}{m_{l}\Lambda_{Q}^{2}}\sim\,\Bigl(\,\frac{1}{r}\,\Bigr)^{(N_{c}-N_{\it l})/N_{c}}\,\Biggl(\frac{m_{\it h}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Biggr)^{(N_{F}-N_{c})/N_{c}}\ll 1\,.\quad\gamma_{+}-\nu_{+}=1\,.\ \

(9.0.1)

The lower energy theory includes : unbroken color
SU(\hat{N}_{c}=N_{c}-N_{\it l})
SYM,
2N_{\it l}N_{\it h}
hybrids
\Pi_{\it hl}+{\Pi}_{\it lh}
,

N_{\it h}
flavors of active
\it h
-quarks with unbroken colors and, finally,
N_{\it l}^{2}
pion fields

\Pi_{\it ll},\,\langle\Pi_{\it ll}\rangle=\langle{\overline{Q}}_{\it l}Q^{l}rangle
. Their Lagrangian at
\mu\lesssim\mu_{{\rm gl},\,L}
we write in the
form (all fields are normalized at
\mu=\Lambda_{Q}
) :

K=z^{+}_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu_{{\rm gl},\,L})\Bigl[K_{\it ll}+K_{\rm hybr}+\dots\Bigr]+z^{+}_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu_{{\rm gl},\,L})K_{\it h},\,\,\,K_{\it h}={\rm Tr}\Bigl({\textsf{Q}}^{\dagger}{\textsf{Q}}+({\textsf{Q}}\rightarrow{\overline{\textsf{Q}}})\Bigr)\,,

K_{\rm hybr}={\rm Tr}\Biggl(\Pi^{\dagger}_{{\it l}{\it h}}\,\frac{1}{\sqrt{\Pi_{\it ll}\Pi^{\dagger}_{\it ll}}}\,\Pi_{{\it l}{\it h}}+\Pi_{{\it h}{\it l}}\,\frac{1}{\sqrt{\Pi^{\dagger}_{\it ll}\Pi_{\it ll}}}\,\Pi^{\dagger}_{{\it h}{\it l}}\Biggr),\quad K_{\it ll}=2\,{\rm Tr}\sqrt{\Pi^{\dagger}_{\it ll}\Pi_{\it ll}}\,\,,

\displaystyle W=\frac{2\pi}{{\hat{\alpha}(\mu)}}\,{\hat{S}}+W_{\Pi}+m_{\it h}{\rm Tr}\,\Bigl({\overline{\textsf{Q}}}{\textsf{Q}}\,\Bigr)\,,\quad W_{\Pi}=m_{\it l}{\rm Tr}\,\Pi_{\it ll}+m_{\it h}{\rm Tr}\,\Bigl(\Pi_{\it hl}\frac{1}{\Pi_{\it ll}}\Pi_{\it lh}\Bigr)\,,

(9.0.2)

where
{\hat{\alpha}}(\mu)
is the gauge coupling of unbroken

SU(N_{c}-N_{l})
(with the pions
\Pi_{\it ll}
sitting down inside),

\hat{S}
is the kinetic term of not higgsed
gluons,
\Pi_{\it ll}
is the
N_{\it l}\times N_{\it l}
matrix of pions
originated due to higgsing of
{\overline{Q}}_{\it l},Q_{\it l}
quarks,

\Pi_{\it lh}
and
\Pi_{\it hl}
are the
N_{\it l}\times N_{\it h}

matrices of the hybrid pions (in essence, these are the quarks
{\overline{Q}}_{\it h},Q_{\it h}
with higgsed colors),
\overline{\textsf{Q}}
and
Q
are
still active
\it h
-quarks with unbroken colors, and dots denote
residual D-term interactions which play no role in what follows and
will be ignored.
z^{+}_{Q}=z_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q}\,,\mu_{{\rm gl},\,L})
in (
9.0.2
) is the
numerical value of the quark renormalization factor due to a perturbative
evolution in the range of scales
\mu_{{\rm gl},\,L}<\mu<\Lambda_{Q}
,

z^{+}_{Q}=z^{+}_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q}\,,\mu_{{\rm gl},\,L})=z^{+}_{Q}(\langle\Pi_{\it ll}\rangle^{\dagger},\langle\Pi_{\it ll}\rangle)\sim\Biggl(\frac{\mu_{{\rm gl},\,L}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Biggr)^{\gamma_{+}}\ll 1\,,\quad\gamma_{+}=(2N_{c}-N_{F})/(N_{F}-N_{c})\,.

The numbers of colors and flavors have already changed in the
threshold region
\mu_{{\rm gl},\,L}/(\rm several)<\mu<(\rm several)\mu_{{\rm gl},\,L}\,,\,N_{F}\rightarrow{\hat{N}}_{F}=N_{F}-N_{\it l}=N_{\it h}\,,\,N_{c}\rightarrow{\hat{N}}_{c}=N_{c}-N_{\it l}
, while the coupling
\hat{\alpha}(\mu)
did not change essentially and remains
\sim\alpha(\mu=\mu_{{\rm gl},\,L})\gg 1
. within this threshold region. Therefore, the
new quark anomalous dimension
\gamma_{-}(\hat{N}_{c}\,,\hat{N}_{F}=N_{\it h}\,,\hat{a}\gg 1)
and the new
\hat{\beta}
-function have the form

\displaystyle\frac{d{\hat{a}}(\mu)}{d\ln\mu}=-\,\,\nu_{-}\,\hat{a}(\mu)\,,\quad\nu_{-}=\frac{\hat{N}_{F}\,\gamma_{-}-{\hat{b}}_{o}}{\hat{N}_{c}}=\frac{3\hat{N}_{c}-2\hat{N}_{F}}{\hat{N}_{F}-\hat{N}_{c}}=\nu_{+}-\frac{N_{\it l}}{N_{F}-N_{c}}\,,

(9.0.3)

{\hat{b}}_{o}=(3\hat{N}_{c}-\hat{N}_{F})=(\rm{b_{o}}-2N_{\it l})>0,,\quad\gamma_{-}=\frac{2\hat{N}_{c}-\hat{N}_{F}}{\hat{N}_{F}-\hat{N}_{c}}=\gamma_{+}-\frac{N_{\it l}}{N_{F}-N_{c}}\,\,.

Depending on the value of
\hat{N}_{F}/\hat{N}_{c}
, the lower energy
theory is in different regimes. We consider here the case with the
strong coupling regime:
1<\hat{N}_{F}/\hat{N}_{c}<3/2
.  In this case,

N_{\it l}<(3N_{c}-2N_{F}),\,\nu_{-}>0
, and hence the coupling
\hat{a}(\mu)

continues to increase with decreasing
\mu
, but more slowly than before.

The next physical scale
\mu^{\prime}_{H}
is given by the pole mass of
active
\overline{\textsf{Q}}\,,\textsf{Q}
quarks

\frac{\mu^{\prime}_{H}}{\Lambda_{Q}}=\frac{m^{\rm pole}_{\it h}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\sim\Biggl[\,\frac{m_{\it h}(\mu=\mu_{{\rm gl},\,L})}{\Lambda_{Q}}\sim\frac{m_{\it h}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Biggl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{\mu_{{\rm gl},\,L}}\Biggr)^{\gamma_{+}}\,\Biggr]\Biggl(\frac{\mu_{{\rm gl},\,L}}{m^{\rm pole}_{\it h}}\Biggr)^{\gamma_{-}}\quad\rightarrow

\displaystyle\frac{m^{\rm pole}_{\it h}}{\mu_{{\rm gl},\,L}}\sim r\ll 1\,,\quad\quad\frac{m^{\rm pole}_{\it h}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\sim\Bigl(\,r\,\Bigr)^{N_{\it l}/N_{c}}\Biggl(\frac{m_{\it h}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Biggr)^{(N_{F}-N_{c})/N_{c}}\gg\frac{{\Lambda}_{SYM}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\,.

(9.0.4)

After integrating out these
\overline{\textsf{Q}}\,,\textsf{Q}
quarks as heavy ones at

\mu<m^{\rm pole}_{\it h}
, we are
left with the
SU(\hat{N}_{c})
SYM theory in the strong-coupling branch

{\hat{a}}_{SYM}(\mu=m^{\rm pole}_{\it h})\gg 1
(and pions). The scale factor of its
gauge coupling
{\hat{\Lambda}}_{SYM}=\langle\Lambda_{L}(\Pi_{\it ll})\rangle
is determined from

\displaystyle{\hat{a}}_{SYM}(\mu=m^{\rm pole}_{\it h})=\Biggl(\frac{m^{\rm pole}_{\it h}}{{\hat{\Lambda}}_{SYM}}\Biggr)^{3}={\hat{a}}(\mu=m^{\rm pole}_{\it h})=\Biggl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{\mu_{{\rm gl},\,L}}\Biggr)^{\nu_{+}}\Biggl(\frac{\mu_{{\rm gl},\,L}}{m^{\rm pole}_{\it h}}\Biggr)^{\nu_{-}}\rightarrow{\hat{\Lambda}}_{SYM}={\Lambda}_{SYM}.

(9.0.5)

Finally, after integrating out
SU(N_{c}-N_{\it l})
gluons at
\mu<{\Lambda}_{SYM}
,
the Lagrangian of pions looks then at
\mu<{\Lambda}_{SYM}
as

\displaystyle\hskip-5.69054pt{\hat{K}}=z^{+}_{Q}\Bigl(K_{\it ll}+K_{\rm hybr}\Bigr),\,\,{\hat{W}}=(N_{c}-N_{\it l})\Biggl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}^{\rm{b_{o}}}m_{\it h}^{N_{\it h}}}{\det\Pi_{\it ll}}\Biggr)^{\frac{1}{(N_{c}-N_{\it l})}}+m_{\it l}{\rm Tr}\,\Pi_{\it ll}+m_{\it h}{\rm Tr}\Bigl(\Pi_{\it hl}\frac{1}{\Pi_{\it ll}}\Pi_{\it lh}\Bigr).\,\,\,

(9.0.6)

From this, the masses of
\Pi_{\it hl}\,,{\Pi}_{\it lh}
and
\Pi_{\it ll}
are

\displaystyle\mu(\Pi_{\it hl})=\mu({\Pi}_{\it lh})\sim\Bigl(r\Bigr)^{\gamma_{-}}\,m^{\rm pole}_{\it h}\,,\quad\mu(\Pi_{\it ll})\sim\frac{m_{\it l}}{z^{+}_{Q}}\sim r\,\mu(\Pi_{\it hl})\,.

(9.0.7)

To check the self-consistency, i.e. that the active
\overline{\textsf{Q}}\,,\textsf{Q}

quarks are indeed in the HQ phase and are not higgsed, we
estimate the possible value of the gluon mass
\mu_{\it gl,\,h}

\displaystyle\mu_{\it gl,\,h}^{2}\sim\Biggl[\,{\hat{a}}(\mu=\mu_{\it gl,\,h})=\Biggl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{\mu_{{\rm gl},\,L}}\Biggr)^{\nu_{+}}\Biggl(\frac{\mu_{{\rm gl},\,L}}{\mu_{\it gl,\,h}}\Biggr)^{\nu_{-}}\,\Biggr]\langle{\overline{\textsf{Q}}}\textsf{Q}\rangle_{\mu=\mu_{\it gl,\,h}}\,\,\,,

(9.0.8)

\displaystyle\frac{\langle{\overline{\textsf{Q}}}\textsf{Q}\rangle_{\mu=\mu_{\it gl,\,h}}}{\Lambda_{Q}^{2}}\sim\,r\,\frac{\mu_{{\rm gl},\,L}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\,\Biggl(\frac{\mu_{{\rm gl},\,L}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Biggr)^{\gamma_{+}}\Biggl(\frac{\mu_{\it gl,\,h}}{\mu_{{\rm gl},\,L}}\Biggr)^{\gamma_{-}}\quad\rightarrow\quad\mu_{\it gl,\,h}\sim m^{\rm pole}_{\it h}\,\,\,,

(9.0.9)

as could be expected. As before, because
N_{h}>{\hat{N}}_{c}
, the
Konishi anomaly and the rank restriction show that
Q
-quarks are

not higgsed
, but confined, see footnote
5
.

As was shown above in the text, in those regions of the parameter
space where an additional small parameter is available (this is

0<\rm{b_{o}}/N_{F}=(3N_{c}-N_{F})/N_{F}\ll 1
at the right
end of the conformal window, or its dual analog

0<\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}/N_{F}=(2N_{F}-3N_{c})/N_{F}\ll 1

at the left end), there are
parametrical differences
in the
mass spectra of direct and dual theories, and therefore they are
clearly not equivalent. In fact, this implies that even
when both
\rm{b_{o}}/N_{F}\sim\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}/N_{F}\sim 1
, there are no reasons for
these two theories to become exactly the same.

12
12

12

But to see the possible differences more clearly, it is insufficient
in this case to make rough estimates of particle masses up to
non-parametric factors
O(1)
as has been done in this paper. One has
either to resolve the mass spectra in more detail or to calculate
some Green’s functions in both theories and to compare them.

Besides, as was shown in section 5, one can trace unavoidable
internal inconsistencies of the Seiberg duality in the variant
”confinement without chiral symmetry breaking”. This variant
implies that at
N_{c}<N_{F}<3N_{c}/2
and in the chiral limit

m_{Q}\rightarrow 0
the direct quarks and gluons
form a large number of massive hadrons with masses

\sim\Lambda_{Q}
, while
new light composite solitons with
masses
\mu_{i}\ll\Lambda_{Q}
appear, described by the dual theory
.

As regards the mass spectra of the direct theory in the dynamical
scenario of this paper, their main features are as follows (for

\rm{b_{o}}/N_{F}=O(1)
, see section 2 for
\rm{b_{o}}/N_{F}\ll 1
).

1) In all cases considered, there is a large number of gluonia with
masses
\sim{\Lambda}_{SYM}=(\Lambda_{Q}^{\rm{b_{o}}}\det m_{Q})^{1/3N_{c}}
.

2) When all quark masses are equal, they are in the HQ phase (i.e.
not higgsed but confined, the string tension
\sqrt{\sigma}\sim{\Lambda}_{SYM}\lesssim m_{Q}^{\rm pole}
), for the whole
interval
N_{c}<N_{F}<3N_{c}
, and hence form a large number of
various hadrons with the mass scales : a)
\sim m_{Q}^{\rm pole}\sim{\Lambda}_{SYM}
at
3N_{c}/2<N_{F}<3N_{c}
, and   b)

\sim m_{Q}^{\rm pole}\sim\Lambda_{Q}(m_{Q}/\Lambda_{Q})^{(N_{F}-N_{c})/N_{c}}\gg{\Lambda}_{SYM}
at

N_{c}<N_{F}<3N_{c}/2
.
There are no additional lighter pions
\pi^{j}_{i}

with masses
\mu_{\pi}\ll m_{Q}^{\rm pole}
, for all
N_{c}<N_{F}<3N_{c}
.

3) The case with
N_{\it l}
flavors of smaller masses
m_{\it l}
and

N_{\it h}=N_{F}-N_{\it l}
flavors
with larger masses
m_{\it h}\,,\,0<m_{\it l}<m_{\it h}\ll\Lambda_{Q}
was also considered.
When
N_{\it l}>N_{c}
, all quarks are also in the HQ phase for all

N_{c}<N_{F}<3N_{c}
, and form a large number of hadrons whose masses
depend on their flavor content , but there are no any additional
lighter pions also.

4) Only when
N_{\it l}<N_{c}
, the
l
- flavored quarks
Q_{\it l}\,,{\overline{Q}}^{\it\overline{l}}
are higgsed,
SU(N_{c})\rightarrow SU(N_{c}-N_{\it l})
, and there
N_{\it l}^{2}

lighter pions
\pi^{\it l}_{\it l}
appear, while the heavier
h
- flavored quarks

Q_{\it h}\,,{\overline{Q}}^{\it h}
always
remain in the HQ phase . In this case, the mass spectra and some new
regimes with unusual properties of the RG flow were presented in
sections 7-9.

We have described above not all possible regimes, but only those that
reveal some qualitatively new features. Some additional details can be
found in
[
20
]
. Besides, we hope that if needed, a reader can deal
with other regimes using the methods from this review.

On the whole, the mass spectra of the direct
{\cal N}=1
SQCD with

SU(N_{c})
colors and
N_{c}<N_{F}<3N_{c}
flavors of massless or light
quarks and its Seiberg’s dual variant with
{\overline{N}}_{c}=N_{F}-N_{c}
dual colors
have been calculated and compared in the framework of simple
dynamical scenario
[
20
]
described in Introduction.

I).  In the region
N_{c}<N_{F}<3N_{c}/2
, as was shown in section 7 of

[
11
]
,
the two-point correlators of conserved currents are different   in the
direct and dual theories in the range of energies
\mu_{H}<\mu<\Lambda_{Q}\,
where all
light quarks in both theories are effectively massless
.

II.)
The parametrical differences in the mass spectra of the
direct and dual theories were traced in the whole range
N_{c}<N_{F}<3N_{c}

for quarks with nonzero masses
.

Let us emphasize that,
in all cases considered, no internal
inconsistences or contradictions with previously proven results were
found in calculations of mass spectra both of the
direct and dual theories within the framework of used scenario from

[
20
]

. Let us recall also that
this dynamical scenario satisfies all
those tests which were used as checks of the Seiberg hypothesis about
the equivalence of the direct and dual theories
. This shows, in particular,
that
all these tests, although necessary, may well be insufficient
.

10

Phase transitions between confinement and higgs phases in
{\cal N}=1

\mathbf{{\cal N}=1\,\,SU(N_{c})}
SQCD with
\mathbf{1\leq N_{F}\leq N_{c}-1}
quark flavors

.

Considered in this section is the standard
{\cal N}=1\,SU(N_{c})
SQCD
with
1\leq N_{F}\leq N_{c}-1
equal mass quark flavors in the fundamental
representation.
The gauge invariant order parameter

\rho
was
introduced in
[
18
]
distinguishing confinement (with
\rho=0
) and
higgs (with
\rho\neq 0
) phases.

Using a number of independent arguments for different variants of
transition between the confinement and higgs regimes, it was shown in

[
18
]
that transitions between these regimes are not crossovers but
the phase transitions. Besides, it was argued that these phase transitions
are of the first order.

This is opposite to the conclusion of the E. Fradkin and S.H. Shenker
paper
[
23
]
that the transition between the confinement and higgs
regimes is the crossover, not the phase transition. And although the
theories considered in
[
18
]
and in
[
23
]
are different, an
experience shows that there is a widely spread opinion
that the conclusion of
[
23
]
is applicable to all QCD-type
theories: both lattice and continuum, both not supersymmetric and
supersymmetric. This opinion is in contradiction with the results of
the paper
[
18
]
.

10.1

Introduction

Considered in
[
18
]
was the standard
{\cal N}=1

SQCD with
SU(N_{c})
colors and
1\leq N_{F}\leq N_{c}-1
flavors of equal mass
quarks with the mass parameter
m_{Q}=m(\mu=\Lambda_{Q})
in the Lagrangian, where

\Lambda_{Q}
is the scale factor of the gauge coupling in the UV region, see e.g.
section 2.1.

The purpose was to show that, in this theory with light quarks with
fixed
m_{Q}\ll\Lambda_{Q}
, there is
the phase transition
from the
region of not too large
N_{c}
where all quarks are higgsed with

\mu_{\rm gl}\gg\Lambda_{Q}
(
10.2.1
), with the gauge invariant order
parameter
\rho_{\rm higgs}\neq 0
, to the region of sufficiently
large
N_{c}
(
10.3.1
) where
\rho_{HQ}=0
and all quarks are in
the HQ (heavy quark) phase and not higgsed but confined.

Besides, it was shown that, at fixed
N_{c}
, there is
the phase
transition
from the region
m_{Q}\gg\Lambda_{Q}
where all heavy quarks are
not higgsed but confined and

\rho_{HQ}=0
, to the region of sufficiently small
m_{Q}\ll\Lambda_{Q}
where
they all are higgsed with
\rho_{\rm higgs}\gg\Lambda_{Q}
.

And finally, using
independent arguments based on realization of
the global flavor symmetry
SU(N_{F})
, it was shown that, at fixed

N_{c}
and
N_{F}=N_{c}-1
, there is

the phase transition
from the region
m_{Q}\gg\Lambda_{Q}
where all
quarks are not higgsed but confined to the region of sufficiently
small
m_{Q}\ll\Lambda_{Q}
where they all are higgsed and not confined.

For all this, let us recall first in short some properties of the
standard
{\cal N}=1
SQCD with
SU(N_{c})
colors and
1\leq N_{F}<3N_{c}
flavors of light equal mass quarks, see e.g. section 2. It is
convenient to start e.g. with
3N_{c}/2<N_{F}<3N_{c}

and the scale
\mu=\Lambda_{Q}
. The Lagrangian looks as

\displaystyle K={\rm Tr}\,\Bigl(Q^{\dagger}e^{V}Q+(Q\rightarrow{\overline{Q}})\Bigr)\,,\quad{\cal W}=\frac{2\pi}{\alpha(\mu=\Lambda_{Q})}S+m_{Q}{\rm Tr}\,({\overline{Q}}Q)\,.

(10.1.1)

Here:
m_{Q}=m_{Q}(\mu=\Lambda_{Q})
is the mass parameter
(taken as real positive),
S=\sum_{A,\beta}W^{A,\,\beta}W^{A}_{\beta}/32\pi^{2}
, where
W^{A}_{\beta}
is the
gauge field strength,
A=1...N_{c}^{2}-1,\,\beta=1,2
,
a(\mu)=N_{c}g^{2}(\mu)/8\pi^{2}=N_{c}\alpha(\mu)/2\pi
is the gauge coupling with its
scale factor
\Lambda_{Q}
. Let us take now
m_{Q}\rightarrow 0
and evolve to the UV
Pauli-Villars (PV) scale
\mu_{PV}
to define the parent UV theory.
The only change in comparison with (
10.1.1
) will be the
appearance of the corresponding logarithmic renormalization factor

z(\Lambda_{Q},\mu_{PV})\gg 1
for
massless
quarks in the Kahler term
and the logarithmic evolution of the gauge coupling:
\alpha(\mu=\Lambda_{Q})\rightarrow\alpha(\mu=\mu_{PV})\ll\alpha(\mu=\Lambda_{Q})
, while the scale factor

\Lambda_{Q}
of the gauge coupling remains the same. Now, the parameter

m_{Q}
is continued from zero to some nonzero value, e.g.
0<m_{Q}\ll\Lambda_{Q}
. And this will be
a definition
of the parent UV theory.

The Konishi anomaly
[
14
]
for this theory looks as
(everywhere below the repeated indices are summed)

\quad m_{Q}(\mu)=z^{-1}_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu)m_{Q}\,,\quad m_{Q}\equiv m_{Q}(\mu=\Lambda_{Q})\,,\quad M^{i}_{j}(\mu)=z_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu)M^{i}_{j}\,,\quad M^{i}_{j}\equiv M^{i}_{j}(\mu=\Lambda_{Q})\,.

\displaystyle m_{Q}(\mu)\langle M^{i}_{j}(\mu)\rangle=\delta^{i}_{j}\langle S\rangle\,,\quad i,j=1...N_{F}\,,\quad\langle M^{i}_{j}\rangle=\langle{\overline{Q}}^{\beta}_{j}Q^{i}_{\beta}\rangle=\delta^{i}_{j}\langle M\rangle\,,\quad\alpha=1...N_{c}\,.

(10.1.2)

Evolving now to lower energies, the regime is conformal at
m^{\rm pole}_{Q}<\mu<\Lambda_{Q}
and the perturbative pole mass of quarks looks as

13
13

13

Here and below the perturbatively exact NSVZ
\beta
-function

[
12
]
is used.
In (
10.1.3
) and below
A\sim B
means equality up to a
constant factor independent of
m_{Q}
and
A\ll B
means
|A|\ll|B|
.

\displaystyle m^{\rm pole}_{Q}=\frac{m_{Q}}{z_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q},m^{\rm pole}_{Q})}\sim\Lambda_{Q}\Bigl(\frac{m_{Q}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\frac{N_{F}}{3N_{c}}}\ll\Lambda_{Q}\,,

(10.1.3)

z_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu\ll\Lambda_{Q})\sim\Bigl(\frac{\mu}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\gamma_{Q}^{\rm conf}=\frac{3N_{c}-N_{F}}{N_{F}}}\ll 1.

Integrating then inclusively all quarks as heavy at
\mu<m^{\rm pole}_{Q}
(
10.1.3
),

14
14

14

It is seen from the Konishi anomaly (
10.1.2
) that

the global flavor symmetry
SU(N_{F})
is not broken spontaneously
in
{\cal N}=1

SU(N_{c})
SQCD
for equal mass quarks
. Therefore, due to the rank restriction at

N_{F}>N_{c}
, all quarks are not higgsed but confined.

there remains
SU(N_{c})
SYM with the scale factor
{\Lambda}_{SYM}

(
10.1.4
) of its
coupling. Integrating then all gluons via the Veneziano-Yankielowicz
(VY) procedure
[
15
]
, one obtains the gluino condensate, see
(
2.1.6
), (
2.1.7
)

\displaystyle{\Lambda}_{SYM}=\Bigl(\Lambda_{Q}^{3N_{c}-N_{F}}m^{N_{F}}_{Q}\Bigr)_{,}^{\frac{1}{3N_{c}}}\quad\langle S\rangle={\Lambda}_{SYM}^{3}=\Bigl(\Lambda_{Q}^{3N_{c}-N_{F}}m^{N_{F}}_{Q}\Bigr)^{\frac{1}{N_{c}}}=m_{Q}\langle M\rangle\,

(10.1.4)

\langle M^{i}_{j}\rangle=\delta^{i}_{j}M\,,\quad M=\Lambda_{Q}^{2}\Bigl(\frac{m_{Q}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\frac{N_{F}-N_{c}}{N_{c}}}\,.

Now (
10.1.4
) can be continued to
1\leq N_{F}<N_{c}

considered in this section.

10.2

The Higgs phase

In this range
1\leq N_{F}<N_{c}
, the weak coupling Higgs phase at

\mu_{\rm gl}\gg\Lambda_{Q}
for light quarks with
0<m_{Q}\ll\Lambda_{Q}
looks as
follows, see e.g. section 2 in
[
11
]
. All quarks are higgsed
i.e.form a constant coherent condensate in a vacuum state, at the high
scale

\mu\sim\mu_{\rm gl}\gg\Lambda_{Q}
in the logarithmic weak coupling regime.

15
15

15

We ignore from now on for simplicity all logarithmic factors and
trace only the power dependence on
m_{Q}/\Lambda_{Q}
and
N_{c}
.

And the perturbative pole masses of
N_{F}(2N_{c}-N_{F})
massive gluons
look as

\Bigl(\frac{\mu_{\rm gl}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{2}\sim g^{2}(\mu=\mu_{\rm gl})\,z_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu=\mu_{\rm gl})\,\frac{\rho_{\rm higgs}^{2}}{\Lambda_{Q}^{2}}\sim\frac{1}{N_{c}}\frac{\langle M\rangle}{\Lambda_{Q}^{2}}\sim

\displaystyle\sim\frac{1}{N_{c}}\frac{\langle S\rangle}{m_{Q}\Lambda_{Q}^{2}}\sim\frac{1}{N_{c}}\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{m_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\frac{N_{c}-N_{F}}{N_{c}}}\gg 1\,.

(10.2.1)

\quad g^{2}(\mu=\mu_{\rm gl})\approx\frac{8\pi^{2}}{(3N_{c}-N_{F})\ln\Bigl(\mu_{\rm gl}/\Lambda_{Q}\Bigr)}\sim\frac{1}{N_{c}}\,,\quad z_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu=\mu_{\rm gl})\sim\Bigl(\ln\frac{\mu_{\rm gl}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\frac{N_{c}}{3N_{c}-N_{F}}}\sim 1\,.

Higgsing of all
N_{F}
quarks with
1\leq N_{F}\leq N_{c}-2
flavors at

\rho_{\rm higgs}=\Lambda_{Q}\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{m_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\frac{N_{c}-N_{F}}{2N_{c}}}\gg\Lambda_{Q}
(
10.2.1
) breaks spontaneously
separately the global
SU(N_{F})

and global
SU(N_{c})
down to
SU(N_{c}-N_{f})
, but there remains
unbroken diagonal
SU(N_{F})_{C+F}
global symmetry. Besides, the gluons
from remained
SU(N_{c}-N_{F})
SYM do not receive large masses

\sim\rho_{higgs}
and remain (effectively) massless at scales

\mu>{\Lambda}_{SYM}
.

Dealing with higgsed quarks, to obtain (
10.2.1
), we first
separate out the Goldstone fields from quark fields
Q^{i}_{\alpha}

normalized at the scale
\Lambda_{Q}
(a part of these Goldstone fields or all
of them will be eaten by gluons when quarks are higgsed)

\displaystyle Q^{i}_{\alpha}(x)=\Bigl(V_{\rm Goldst}^{SU(N_{c})}(x)\Bigr)^{\beta}_{\alpha}\,{\hat{Q}}^{i}_{\beta}(x)\,,\quad{\hat{Q}}^{i}_{\beta}(x)=\Bigl(V_{\rm Goldst}^{SU(N_{c})}(x)^{\dagger}\Bigr)^{\gamma}_{\beta}Q^{i}_{\gamma}(x)\,,

(10.2.2)

{\hat{Q}}^{i}_{\beta}(x)=\Bigl(U_{\rm global}^{SU(N_{c})}\Bigr)^{\delta}_{\beta}\Bigl(U_{\rm global}^{SU(N_{F})}\Bigr)^{i}_{j}{\tilde{Q}}^{j}_{\delta}(x),\quad\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta=1...N_{c},\,\,i,j=1...N_{F},

where
V_{\rm Goldst}^{SU(N_{c})}(x)
is the
N_{c}\times N_{c}
unitary

SU(N_{c})
matrix of Goldstone fields.

But the physical degrees of freedom of massive scalar superpartners
of   massive gluons and light pion fields
\Pi^{i}_{j}
(
2.1.13
),
originating   from combined physical degrees of freedom of
Q

and
{\overline{Q}}
,
remain in
{\hat{Q}}^{i}_{\beta}(x)
and
{\hat{\overline{Q}}_{i}^{\,\beta}(x)}
.

This can be checked by direct counting. The quark fields
Q
and
{\overline{Q}}
have
4N_{F}N_{c}
real physical degrees of freedom on the whole. From
these,
N_{F}(2N_{c}-N_{F})
real Goldstone degrees of freedom are eaten by
massive gluons. (The extra
(N_{c}-N_{F})^{2}-1
real Goldstone modes not
eaten by remaining perturbatively massless
SU(N_{c}-N_{F})
gluons remain
not physical due to the gauge invariance of the Lagrangian). The same
number
N_{F}(2N_{c}-N_{F})
of combined real physical degrees of freedom
of
Q
and
{\overline{Q}}
form scalar superpartners of massive gluons. And remaining

2N_{F}^{2}
combined real degrees of freedom of
Q
and
{\overline{Q}}
form

N_{F}^{2}
complex physical degrees of freedom of light colorless pions
\Pi^{i}_{j}
.

And then, with the standard choice of vacuum of spontaneously broken
global symmetry, we replace
{\hat{Q}}^{i}_{\beta}(x)
in (
10.2.2
),
containing remained degrees of freedom, by its mean vacuum value
(at
\mu=\Lambda_{Q}
)

\displaystyle\langle{\hat{Q}}^{i}_{\beta}(x)\rangle=\langle{\hat{Q}}^{i}_{\beta}(0)\rangle=\delta^{i}_{\beta}\,\rho_{\rm higgs},\,\,\frac{\rho_{\rm higgs}}{\Lambda_{Q}}=\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{m_{Q}}\,\Bigr)^{\frac{N_{c}-N_{F}}{2N_{c}}}\gg 1,\,\,i=1...N_{F},\,\,\beta=1...N_{c}\,.

(10.2.3)

And similarly
\langle{\hat{\overline{Q}}_{i}^{\,\beta}(x)}\rangle=\delta_{i}^{\,\beta}\,\rho_{\rm higgs}
.

Under pure gauge transformations, see (
10.2.2
) :

\displaystyle Q^{i}_{\alpha}(x)\rightarrow\Bigl(V_{\rm pure\,gauge}^{SU(N_{c})}(x)\Bigr)^{\beta}_{\alpha}Q^{i}_{\beta}(x),\quad V_{\rm Goldst}^{SU(N_{c})}(x)\rightarrow V_{\rm pure\,gauge}^{SU(N_{c})}(x)V_{\rm Goldst}^{SU(N_{c})}(x).

(10.2.4)

That is, these are
Q^{i}_{\alpha}(x)
and Goldstone fields which are
transformed in (
10.2.2
),(
10.2.4
), while
{\hat{Q}}^{i}_{\beta}(x)
stays intact under pure gauge transformations and is
the gauge invariant quark field. And
\rho_{\rm higgs}\neq 0
in
(
10.2.3
) is the
gauge invariant order parameter
for
higgsed scalar quarks, while

\rho_{\rm HQ}=0
if quarks are in the HQ (heavy quark) phase and not
higgsed, see (
11.1.1
),(
11.1.2
). ( And, in particular,
V_{\rm pure\,gauge}^{SU(N_{c})}(x)V_{\rm Goldst}^{SU(N_{c})}(x)=I_{SU(N_{c})}

in the so called ”unitary gauge”  where
I_{SU(N_{c})}
is the unity matrix).

Under the replacement
Q\rightarrow V_{\rm Goldst}^{SU(N_{c})}(x){\hat{Q}}

(
10.2.2
), the covariant derivative
