{\overline{g}}(\mu=\Lambda_{Q})
, i.e.

\mu_{2}/\mu_{1}\lesssim 1
. The same requirement to the value of
the Yukawa coupling follows from the conformal behavior of this
theory at
3/2<N_{F}/N_{c}<2
and
\mu<\Lambda_{Q}
, i.e.

{\overline{f}}(\mu=\Lambda_{Q})=1=\mu_{2}/\mu_{1}\approx f_{*}
at
\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}/N_{F}=O(1)
.
We consider below this dual theory at
\mu\leq\Lambda_{Q}
only, where it
claims to be equivalent to the direct
\Phi
- theory. As was
explained in
[
20
]
, one has to take
\mu_{1}\sim\Lambda_{Q}
at

\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}/N_{F}=(3{\overline{N}}_{c}-N_{F})/N_{F}=O(1)
in (
14.2.1
) to match the
gluino condensates in the direct and dual theories. Therefore,

\mu_{2}\sim\mu_{1}\sim\Lambda_{Q}
in this case also. But to match the
gluino condensates in the direct and dual theories at the left
end of the conformal window, i.e. at
0<\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}/N_{F}\ll 1
, one has
to take
(\mu_{2}/\mu_{1})^{2}\approx f^{2}_{*}=O(\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}/N_{F})\ll 1
and

\mu_{1}\sim Z_{q}\Lambda_{Q}\ll\Lambda_{Q},\,Z_{q}\sim\exp\{-{\overline{N}}_{c}/7\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}\}\ll 1
(with
the exponential accuracy, i.e. powers of the small parameter

0<\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}/N_{F}\ll 1
are not traced here and only the powers of

Z_{q}
are traced, this is sufficient for our purposes, so that
at
\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}/N_{F}=O(1)
one has to put
Z_{q}\rightarrow 1
, see
[
20
]

for more details.

Really, the fields
\Phi
remain always too heavy and
dynamically irrelevant in this
d\Phi
- theory at
3N_{c}/2<N_{F}<2N_{c}
, so that they can be integrated out once and
forever and, finally, we write the Lagrangian of the dual theory
at
\mu=\Lambda_{Q}
in the form

K={\rm Tr}\Bigl(q^{\dagger}q+(q\rightarrow\overline{q})\Bigr)+{\rm Tr}\,\frac{M^{\dagger}M}{Z^{2}_{q}\Lambda_{Q}^{2}}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt{\cal W}=\,\,\frac{2\pi}{\overline{\alpha}(\mu=\Lambda_{Q})}\,{\overline{s}}+{\cal W}_{M}+{\cal W}_{q}\,,

\displaystyle{\cal W}_{M}=m_{Q}{\rm Tr}\,M-\frac{1}{2\mu_{\Phi}}\Biggl[{\rm Tr}\,(M^{2})-\frac{1}{N_{c}}({\rm Tr}\,M)^{2}\Biggr]\,,\hskip 11.74988pt{\cal W}_{q}=-\,\frac{1}{Z_{q}\Lambda_{Q}}\,\rm{Tr}\Bigl({\overline{q}}\,M\,q\Bigr)\,.

(14.2.2)

The Konishi anomalies for the
i
-th flavor look here as (
{\it i}=1\,...\,N_{F}
)

\displaystyle\langle M_{i}\rangle\langle N_{i}\rangle=Z_{q}\Lambda_{Q}\langle S\rangle\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\frac{\langle N_{i}\rangle}{Z_{q}\Lambda_{Q}}=m_{Q}-\frac{1}{\mu_{\Phi}}\Bigl(\langle M_{i}-\frac{1}{N_{c}}{\rm Tr}\,M\rangle\Bigr)=\langle m_{Q,i}^{\rm tot}\rangle\,,

(14.2.3)

\langle N_{i}\rangle\equiv\langle{\overline{q}}^{i}q_{i}(\mu=\Lambda_{Q})\rangle\,,\hskip 11.74988pt{\rm no\,\,summation\,\,over\,\,i}\,.

In vacua with the broken flavor symmetry these can be rewritten
as

\langle M_{1}+M_{2}-\frac{1}{N_{c}}{\rm Tr}\,M\rangle_{\rm br}=m_{Q}\mu_{\Phi},\hskip 11.74988pt\langle S\rangle_{\rm br}=\frac{1}{\mu_{\Phi}}\langle M_{1}\rangle_{\rm br}\langle M_{2}\rangle_{\rm br},\hskip 11.74988pt\langle M_{1}\rangle_{\rm br}\neq\langle M_{2}\rangle_{\rm br}\,,

\displaystyle\frac{\langle N_{1}\rangle_{\rm br}}{Z_{q}\Lambda_{Q}}=\frac{\langle S\rangle_{\rm br}}{\langle M_{1}\rangle_{\rm br}}=\frac{\langle M_{2}\rangle_{\rm br}}{\mu_{\Phi}}=m_{Q}-\frac{1}{\mu_{\Phi}}\Bigl(\langle M_{1}-\frac{1}{N_{c}}{\rm Tr}\,M\rangle_{\rm br}\Bigr)=\langle m^{\rm tot}_{Q,1}\rangle_{\rm br}\,,

(14.2.4)

\frac{\langle N_{2}\rangle_{\rm br}}{Z_{q}\Lambda_{Q}}=\frac{\langle S\rangle_{\rm br}}{\langle M_{2}\rangle_{\rm br}}=\frac{\langle M_{1}\rangle_{\rm br}}{\mu_{\Phi}}=m_{Q}-\frac{1}{\mu_{\Phi}}\Bigl(\langle M_{2}-\frac{1}{N_{c}}{\rm Tr}\,M\rangle_{\rm br}\Bigr)=\langle m^{\rm tot}_{Q,1}\rangle_{\rm br}\,.

15

Vacua, condensates and mass spectra at
\mathbf{0<N_{F}<N_{c}}

Clearly, there is no dual theory for this range of

N_{F}
values. Moreover (see below in this section), in vacua of
the direct theory with the unbroken flavor symmetry all quarks
are higgsed in the logarithmically small coupling regime
with the large masses of higgsed gluons,
\mu_{\rm gl}\gg\Lambda_{Q}

(at not too large values of
N_{c}
, see
[
18
]
).
In vacua with the broken flavor symmetry all quarks: a) are
either also higgsed in the weak (logarithmically small) coupling
regime with
\mu_{\rm gl,\,\it i}\gg\Lambda_{Q},\,i=1,2
, at

\Lambda_{Q}\ll\mu_{\Phi}\ll{\tilde{\mu}}_{\Phi}
,
{\tilde{\mu}}_{\Phi}\sim\Lambda_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q}/m_{Q})^{(\rm{b_{o}}-n_{1})/n_{1}}\gg\Lambda_{Q}
;   b) or, in
\rm br1
-vacua with
1\leq n_{1}\leq[N_{F}/2]
, the quarks
{\overline{Q}}_{1},\,Q^{1}
with flavors
n_{1}
are higgsed in the weak
(logarithmically small) coupling regime, while the quarks
{\overline{Q}}_{2},\,Q^{2}
with flavors
n_{2}=N_{F}-n_{1}
are in the HQ-phase at

\mu_{\Phi}\gg{\tilde{\mu}}_{\Phi}
, they are weakly confined (i.e. the
tension of confining string originated from unbroken

SU(N_{c}-n_{1})
color group is much smaller than quark masses,

\sqrt{\sigma}\ll m_{Q,\,perturb}^{\rm pole}
) and also
perturbatively logarithmically weakly coupled and
non-relativistic inside hadrons (in
\rm br2
-vacua

n_{1}\leftrightarrow n_{2}
). Therefore, finally, in all vacua and
at all values
\Lambda_{Q}\ll\mu_{\Phi}
, the quarks are parametrically weakly
coupled and their dynamics is simple and qualitatively evident.

For this reason,
we need no any additional assumptions
about the quark dynamics to calculate the mass spectra at

0<N_{F}<N_{c}
. In other words, because the HQ- and Higgs-phases of
quarks are at logarithmically weak couplings, there is no need
to mention about any assumed dynamical scenario at all (it is
really needed to calculate the mass spectra in the strong
coupling region only).

The calculations methods used below in this section have much in
common with those in the standard SQCD with
m_{Q}/\Lambda_{Q}\ll 1
and

0<N_{F}<N_{c}
in section 2 of
[
11
]
. It is implied that the
reader is familiar with
[
11
]
, so that some technical ins
and outs are omitted below (see section 2 in
[
11
]
for much
more details). But really, as mentioned above, because all
quarks are parametrically weakly coupled, all calculations in
this section 15 are highly standard and, we hope, self-evident.

15.1

Unbroken flavor symmetry

There is
N_{\rm unbrok}=(2N_{c}-N_{F})
such vacua and all quarks
are higgsed in all of them, but the hierarchies in the mass
spectrum are parametrically different depending on the value of

\mu_{\Phi}
(see below). In any case, all
N_{F}^{2}
fions are very
heavy and dynamically irrelevant in these vacua at scales

\mu<\mu^{\rm pole}_{1}(\Phi)=f^{2}\mu_{\Phi}
and can be
integrated out from the beginning.

All quarks are higgsed at the high scale
\mu=\mu_{\rm gl},\,\Lambda_{Q}\ll\mu_{\rm gl}\ll\mu^{\rm pole}_{1}(\Phi)=\mu_{\Phi}/z_{\Phi}\simeq\mu_{\Phi}
,

\displaystyle\mu^{2}_{\rm gl}\sim g^{2}(\mu=\mu_{\rm gl})z_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu_{\rm gl})\langle\Pi\rangle,\hskip 11.74988pt\langle\Pi\rangle=\langle{\overline{Q}}_{1}Q^{1}(\mu=\Lambda_{Q})\rangle\equiv\langle{\overline{Q}}Q\rangle,\hskip 11.74988ptg^{2}(\mu)=4\pi\alpha(\mu),

(15.1.1)

where (in the approximation of leading logs and

C_{F}=(N_{c}^{2}-1)/2N_{c}\approx N_{c}/2
)

\displaystyle\frac{2\pi}{\alpha(\mu_{\rm gl})}\approx\rm{b_{o}}\ln{\frac{\mu_{\rm gl}}{\Lambda_{Q}}}\,\,,\,\,z_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu_{\rm gl})\sim\Bigl(\frac{\alpha(\Lambda_{Q})}{\alpha(\mu_{\rm gl})}\Bigr)^{2C_{F}/\rm{b_{o}}}\sim\Bigl(\ln\frac{\mu_{\rm gl}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{N_{c}/\rm{b_{o}}}\gg 1\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\rm{b_{o}}=3N_{c}-N_{F}\,.

(15.1.2)

Hence, after integrating out all heavy higgsed gluons and their
superpartners at
\mu<\mu_{\rm gl}
one remains with the

SU(N_{c}-N_{F})
pure Yang-Mills theory with the scale factor

{\Lambda}_{SYM}
of its gauge coupling. Finally, after integrating out
remained gluons at
\mu<{\Lambda}_{SYM}
via the Veneziano-Yankielowicz
(VY) procedure
[
15
]
(see section 2 in
[
11
]
for
more details), one obtains the Lagrangian of
N_{F}^{2}
pions

\displaystyle K=2z_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu_{\rm gl})\,{\rm Tr}\,\sqrt{\Pi^{\dagger}\Pi}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt{\cal W}={\overline{N}}_{c}S+{\cal W}_{\Pi}\,,

(15.1.3)

S=\Biggl(\,\frac{\Lambda_{Q}^{\rm{b_{o}}}}{\det\Pi}\,\Biggr)^{\frac{1}{N_{c}-N_{F}}}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt{\cal W}_{\Pi}=m_{Q}{\rm Tr}\,\Pi-\frac{1}{2\mu_{\Phi}}\Biggl[{\rm Tr}\,({\Pi}^{2})-\frac{1}{N_{c}}({\rm Tr}\,\Pi)^{2}\Biggr],

\langle\Pi^{i}_{j}\rangle=\delta^{i}_{j}\,\langle\Pi\rangle=\delta^{i}_{j}\,\langle{\overline{Q}}_{1}Q^{1}(\mu=\Lambda_{Q})\rangle,\hskip 11.74988pti,j=1\,...\,N_{F}\,.

It follows from (
15.1.3
) that depending on the value of

\mu_{\Phi}/\Lambda_{Q}\gg 1
there are two different regimes.

i)   At
\Lambda_{Q}\ll\mu_{\Phi}\ll m_{1}=\Lambda_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q}/m_{Q})^{(2N_{c}-N_{F})/N_{c}},\,\,m_{1}\gg\Lambda_{Q}
, the term

m_{Q}{\rm Tr}({\overline{Q}}Q)
in the superpotential (
15.1.3
)
gives only small correction and one obtains

\displaystyle\langle\Pi\rangle_{\rm o}\sim\Lambda_{Q}^{2}\Bigl(\frac{\mu_{\Phi}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Biggr)^{\frac{N_{c}-N_{F}}{2N_{c}-N_{F}}}\gg\Lambda_{Q}^{2}\,.

(15.1.4)

There are
(2N_{c}-N_{F})
such vacua, this agrees with
[
21
]
.

31
31

31

To see that there are just
2N_{c}-N_{F}
vacua and not less, one
has to separate slightly all quark masses,
m_{Q}\rightarrow m_{Q}^{(i)},\,i=1...N_{F},\,0<(\delta m_{Q})^{ij}=(m_{Q}^{(i)}-m_{Q}^{(j)})\ll{\overline{m}}_{Q}
. All quark mass
terms give only small power corrections to (
15.1.4
), but just these
corrections show the
Z_{2N_{c}-N_{F}}
multiplicity of vacua.

The masses of heavy gluons and their superpartners are given in
(
15.1.1
), while from (
15.1.3
) the pion masses are

\displaystyle\mu_{\rm o}(\Pi)\sim\frac{\langle\Pi\rangle_{\rm o}}{z_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu_{\rm gl})\mu_{\Phi}}\sim\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{z_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu_{\rm gl})}\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\Biggr)^{\frac{N_{c}}{2N_{c}-N_{F}}}\ll m_{Q}\,.

(15.1.5)

Besides, the scale of the gluino condensate of unbroken

SU(N_{c}-N_{F})
is

\displaystyle{\Lambda}_{SYM}=\langle S\rangle^{1/3}\sim\Biggl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}^{\rm{b_{o}}}}{\det\langle\Pi\rangle_{\rm o}}\Biggr)^{\frac{1}{3(N_{c}-N_{F})}}\sim\Lambda_{Q}\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\Biggr)^{\frac{N_{F}}{3(2N_{c}-N_{F})}},\,\,\mu_{\rm o}(\Pi)\ll{\Lambda}_{SYM}\ll\Lambda_{Q}\ll\mu_{\rm gl},\,\,\,\,

(15.1.6)

and there is a large number of gluonia with the mass scale
\sim{\Lambda}_{SYM}
(except for the case
N_{F}=N_{c}-1
when the whole gauge
group is higgsed, there is no confinement and no gluonia with
masses
\sim{\Lambda}_{SYM}

and the non-perturbative superpotential in (
15.1.3
)
originates not from the unbroken
SU(N_{c}-N_{F})
but directly from
the instanton contribution
[
19
]
).

ii)
(2N_{c}-N_{F})
vacua split into two groups of
vacua with parametrically different mass spectra at
\mu_{\Phi}\gg m_{1}
. There are
N_{c}
SQCD vacua with
\langle\Pi\rangle_{\rm SQCD}\sim\Lambda_{Q}^{2}(\Lambda_{Q}/m_{Q})^{(N_{c}-N_{F})/N_{c}}
differing
by
Z_{N_{c}}
phases of the quark and gluino condensates (in
these, the last term
\sim\Pi^{2}/\mu_{\Phi}
in the superpotential
(
15.1.3
) can be neglected). And
(N_{c}-N_{F})
of nearly
degenerate classical vacua with parametrically larger
condensates
\langle\Pi\rangle_{\rm cl}\sim m_{Q}\mu_{\Phi}
. In
these, the first non-perturbative quantum term
\sim S
in the
superpotential (
15.1.3
) gives only small corrections with

Z_{N_{c}-N_{F}}
phases, but the multiplicity of vacua originates
just from these small corrections. The properties of SQCD vacua
have been described in detail in chapter 2 of
[
11
]
, the
pion masses are
\mu_{\rm SQCD}(\Pi)\sim m_{Q}/z_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu^{SQCD}_{\rm gl})\ll m_{Q}
therein, where

z_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu^{SQCD}_{\rm gl})\gg 1
is the logarithically large
perturbative renormalization factor. In
(N_{c}-N_{F})
classical
vacua the gluon and pion masses are given in
(
15.1.5
) but now

\displaystyle\langle\Pi\rangle_{\rm cl}\sim m_{Q}\mu_{\Phi}\gg\Lambda_{Q}^{2}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\mu_{\rm cl}(\Pi)\sim\frac{m_{Q}}{z_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu^{\rm cl}_{\rm gl})}\ll m_{Q}\,,

(15.1.7)

and in all vacua (except for the case
N_{F}=N_{c}-1
) there is a
large number of gluonia with the mass scale

\displaystyle\sim{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{\rm SQCD}=\langle S\rangle^{1/3}\sim\Biggl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}^{\rm{b_{o}}}}{\det\langle\Pi\rangle_{\rm SQCD}}\Biggr)^{\frac{1}{3(N_{c}-N_{F})}}\sim\Lambda_{Q}\Biggl(\frac{m_{Q}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Biggr)^{N_{F}/3N_{c}}\hskip 11.74988pt{\rm in\,\,N_{c}\,\,\,SQCD\,\,vacua}\,,

(15.1.8)

\displaystyle\sim{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{\rm class}\sim\Biggl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}^{\rm{b_{o}}}}{\det\langle\Pi\rangle_{\rm cl}}\Biggr)^{\frac{1}{3(N_{c}-N_{F})}}\sim\Lambda_{Q}\Biggl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}^{2}}{m_{Q}\mu_{\Phi}}\Biggr)^{\frac{N_{F}}{3(N_{c}-N_{F})}}\,\,{\rm in\,\,(N_{c}-N_{F})\,\,classical\,\,vacua}.

(15.1.9)

Finally, the change of regimes
{\bf i}\leftrightarrow{\bf ii}

occurs at

\displaystyle\Biggl(\frac{m_{1}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Biggr)^{\frac{N_{c}-N_{F}}{2N_{c}-N_{F}}}\sim\frac{m_{Q}m_{1}}{\Lambda_{Q}^{2}}\gg 1\hskip 11.74988pt\rightarrow\hskip 11.74988ptm_{1}\sim\Lambda_{Q}\Biggl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{m_{Q}}\Biggr)^{\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{N_{c}}}\gg\Lambda_{Q}\,.

(15.1.10)

15.2

Spontaneously broken flavor symmetry :
U(N_{F})\rightarrow U(n_{1})\times U(n_{2})

The quark condensates
\langle{\overline{Q}}_{j}Q^{i}\rangle\sim\delta^{i}_{j}C_{i}
split into two groups in these
vacua with the spontaneously broken flavor symmetry : there are

1\leq n_{1}\leq[N_{F}/2]
equal values

\langle\Pi_{1}\rangle=\langle{\overline{Q}}_{1}Q^{1}\rangle\equiv\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle
and
n_{2}=(N_{F}-n_{1})\geq n_{1}

equal values
\langle\Pi_{2}\rangle=\langle{\overline{Q}}_{2}Q^{2}\rangle\equiv\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle\neq\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle
(unless
stated explicitly, here and everywhere below in the text it is
implied that
1-(n_{1}/N_{c}),\,\,1-(n_{2}/N_{c})
and
(2N_{c}-N_{F})/N_{c}

are all
O(1)
). And there will be two different phases,
depending on the value of
\mu_{\Phi}/\Lambda_{Q}\gg 1
(see below).

At
\Lambda_{Q}\ll\mu_{\Phi}\ll m_{1}
all qualitative
properties are similar to those for unbroken symmetry. All
quarks are higgsed at high scales
\mu_{\rm gl,1}\sim\mu_{\rm gl,2}\gg\Lambda_{Q}
and the low energy Lagrangian has the form
(
15.1.3
). The term
m_{Q}{\rm Tr}({\overline{Q}}Q)
in the
superpotential in (
15.1.3
) gives only small corrections,
while (
14.1.4
) can be rewritten here in the form

\langle\Pi_{1}+\Pi_{2}\rangle_{\rm br}=\frac{1}{N_{c}}{\rm Tr}\,\langle\Pi\rangle_{\rm br}+m_{Q}\mu_{\Phi}\approx\frac{1}{N_{c}}\langle n_{1}\Pi_{1}+n_{2}\Pi_{2}\rangle_{\rm br}\,\,\rightarrow\,\,\Bigl(1-\frac{n_{1}}{N_{c}}\Bigr)\langle\Pi_{1}\rangle_{\rm br}\approx-\Bigl(1-\frac{n_{2}}{N_{c}}\Bigr)\langle\Pi_{2}\rangle_{\rm br},

\displaystyle\langle S\rangle_{\rm br}=\Biggl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}^{\rm{b_{o}}}}{\langle\Pi_{1}\rangle^{n_{1}}_{\rm br}\langle\Pi_{2}\rangle^{n_{2}}_{\rm br}}\Biggr)^{\frac{1}{N_{c}-N_{F}}}=\frac{\langle\Pi_{1}\rangle_{\rm br}\langle\Pi_{2}\rangle_{\rm br}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\,,

(15.2.1)

\displaystyle\mu^{2}_{\rm gl,1}\sim\mu^{2}_{\rm gl,2}\sim g^{2}(\mu=\mu_{\rm gl})z_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu_{\rm gl})\langle\Pi_{1,2}\rangle_{\rm br}\gg\Lambda_{Q}^{2},\,\,\,\,\langle\Pi_{1}\rangle_{\rm br}\sim\langle\Pi_{2}\rangle_{\rm br}\sim\Lambda_{Q}^{2}\Biggl(\frac{\mu_{\Phi}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Biggr)^{\frac{N_{c}-N_{F}}{2N_{c}-N_{F}}}\,.

(15.2.2)

The pion masses in this regime look as follows, see
(
15.2.7
) :  a) due to the spontaneous breaking of the
flavor symmetry,
U(N_{F})\rightarrow U(n_{1})\times U(n_{2})
, there always
will be
2n_{1}n_{2}
exactly massless Nambu-Goldstone particles
and in this case these are the hybrids
\Pi_{12}
and

\Pi_{21}
;  b) other
n_{1}^{2}+n_{2}^{2}
‘normal’ pions have
masses as in (
15.2.7
).

There are

\displaystyle N^{\rm tot}_{\rm brok}=\sum_{n_{1}=1}^{n_{1}=[N_{F}/2]}N_{\rm brok}(n_{1})=\sum_{n_{1}=1}^{n_{1}=[N_{F}/2]}(2N_{c}-N_{F}){\overline{C}}^{\,n_{1}}_{N_{F}}\,,\hskip 11.74988ptC^{\,n_{1}}_{N_{F}}=\frac{N_{F}!}{n_{1}!\,n_{2}!}

(15.2.3)

such vacua (the factor
2N_{c}-N_{F}
originates from

Z_{2N_{c}-N_{F}}
, for even
N_{F}
the last term with

n_{1}=N_{F}/2
enters (
15.2.3
) with the additional factor

1/2
, i.e.
{\overline{C}}^{\,n_{1}}_{N_{F}}
differ from the standard

C^{\,n_{1}}_{N_{F}}
only by
{\overline{C}}^{\,n_{1}={\rm k}}_{N_{F}=2{\rm k}}=C^{\,n_{1}={\rm k}}_{N_{F}=2{\rm k}}/2
), so
that the total number of vacua is

32
32

32

By convention, we ignore the continuous multiplicity of vacua
due to the spontaneous flavor symmetry breaking. Another way,
one can separate slightly all quark masses,
so that all Nambu-Goldstone bosons will acquire small masses

O(\delta m_{Q})\ll{\overline{m}}_{Q}
.

\displaystyle N_{\rm tot}=\Bigl(N_{\rm unbrok}=2N_{c}-N_{F}\Bigr)+N^{\rm tot}_{\rm brok}\,,

(15.2.4)

this agrees with
[
21
]
.

The change of the regime in these vacua
with the broken symmetry occurs at

m_{1}\ll\mu_{\Phi}\ll{\tilde{\mu}}_{\Phi}
, see
(
15.2.10
), when all quarks are still higgsed
but there appears a large hierarchy between the values of quark
condensates at
\mu_{\Phi}\gg m_{1}
, see (
15.2.5
). Instead of

\langle\Pi_{1}\rangle\sim\langle\Pi_{2}\rangle
, they look
now as:

a)
\rm{br}1
(
\rm{br}1=\rm{breaking}-1
) - vacua

\displaystyle\hskip-11.38109pt\langle\Pi_{1}\rangle_{\rm br1}\approx\Biggl(\rho_{1}=\frac{N_{c}}{N_{c}-n_{1}}\Biggr)m_{Q}\mu_{\Phi}\gg\Lambda_{Q}^{2},\,\,\langle\Pi_{2}\rangle_{\rm br1}\approx\Lambda_{Q}^{2}\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{m_{Q}\rho_{1}}\Bigr)^{\frac{N_{c}-n_{2}}{N_{c}-n_{1}}}\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\Bigr)^{\frac{n_{1}}{N_{c}-n_{1}}}\ll\langle\Pi_{1}\rangle_{\rm br1}.\,\,\,\,

(15.2.5)

Unlike the mainly quantum
\langle\Pi\rangle_{\rm o}
or mainly
classical
\langle\Pi\rangle_{\rm cl}
vacua with unbroken
symmetry, these vacua are pseudo-classical : the largest value
of the condensate
\langle\Pi_{1}\rangle_{\rm br1}\sim m_{Q}\mu_{\Phi}

is classical while the smaller value of
\langle\Pi_{2}\rangle_{\rm br1}\sim\langle S\rangle_{\rm br1}/m_{Q}
is of
quantum origin. There are
N_{\rm br1}(n_{1})=(N_{c}-n_{1}){\overline{C}}_{N_{F}}^{\,n_{1}}
such vacua at given
values of
n_{1}
and
n_{2}
.

b)
\rm{br2}
- vacua. These are obtained from
(
15.2.5
) by
n_{1}\leftrightarrow n_{2}
and there are
N_{\rm br2}(n_{2})=(N_{c}-n_{2}){\overline{C}}_{N_{F}}^{\,n_{2}}
such vacua. Of
course, the total number of vacua,
N_{\rm brok}=N_{\rm br1}(n_{1})+N_{\rm br2}(n_{2})=(2N_{c}-N_{F}){\overline{C}}_{N_{F}}^{\,n_{1}}

remains the same at
\mu_{\Phi}\lessgtr m_{1}
.

We consider
\rm br1
vacua (all results in
\rm br2
vacua can
be obtained by
n_{1}\leftrightarrow n_{2}
). In the range

m_{1}\ll\mu_{\Phi}\ll{\tilde{\mu}}_{\Phi}
(see below) where all quarks
are higgsed finally, the masses of higgsed gluons look now as

\displaystyle\mu^{2}_{\rm gl,1}\sim g^{2}(\mu=\mu_{\rm gl,1})z_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu_{\rm gl,1})\langle\Pi_{1}\rangle\gg\mu^{2}_{\rm gl,2}\,.

(15.2.6)

The superpotential in the low energy Lagrangian of pions looks
as in (
15.1.3
), but the Kahler term of pions is different.
We write it in the form  :
K\sim z_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu_{\rm gl,1}){\rm Tr}\sqrt{\Pi^{\dagger}_{z}\Pi_{z}}
. The
N_{F}\times N_{F}
matrix

\Pi_{z}
of pions looks
as follows. Its
n_{2}\times n_{2}
part consists of fields

z^{\prime}_{Q}(\mu_{\rm gl,1},\mu_{\rm gl,2})\Pi_{22}
, where

z^{\prime}_{Q}\ll 1
is the perturbative logarithmic
renormalization factor of
{\overline{\textsf{Q}}}_{2},\,{\textsf{Q}}^{2}
quarks with
not higgsed colors which appears due to their additional RG
evolution in the range of scales
\mu_{\rm gl,2}<\mu<\mu_{\rm gl,1}
, while at
\mu=\mu_{\rm gl,2}
they are also higgsed. All
other pion fields
\Pi_{11},\Pi_{12}
and
\Pi_{21}
are
normal. As a result, the pion masses look as follows.
2n_{1}n_{2}

hybrid pions
\Pi_{12}
and
\Pi_{21}
are massless, while the
masses of
n_{1}^{2}

\Pi_{11}
and
n_{2}^{2}

\Pi_{22}
are

\displaystyle\mu(\Pi_{11})\sim\frac{m_{Q}}{z_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu_{\rm gl,1})}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\mu(\Pi_{22})\sim\frac{m_{Q}}{z_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu_{\rm\,gl,1})z^{\prime}_{Q}(\mu_{\rm gl,1},\mu_{\rm gl,2})}\,,

(15.2.7)

where
z_{Q}
and
z^{\rm\prime}_{Q}
are both logarithmic.
Finally, the mass scale of gluonia from the not higgsed

SU(N_{c}-N_{F})
group is
\sim{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{\rm(br1)}
, where

\displaystyle({\Lambda}_{SYM}^{\rm(br1)})^{3}=\langle S\rangle_{\rm br1}=\frac{\langle\Pi_{1}\rangle_{\rm br1}\langle\Pi_{2}\rangle_{\rm br1}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\sim m_{Q}\langle\Pi_{2}\rangle_{\rm br1}\sim\Lambda_{Q}^{3}\Biggl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\Biggr)^{\frac{n_{1}}{N_{c}-n_{1}}}\Biggl(\frac{m_{Q}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Biggr)^{\frac{n_{2}-n_{1}}{N_{c}-n_{1}}}\,.

(15.2.8)

At scales
\mu_{\rm gl,2}\ll\mu<\mu_{\rm gl,1}\sim\langle\Pi_{1}\rangle^{1/2}\sim(m_{Q}\mu_{\Phi})^{1/2}
(ignoring
logarithmic factors) the light degrees of freedom include :  a)

SU(N_{c}-n_{1})
gluons,  b) active quarks
{\overline{\textsf{Q}}}_{2},\,{\textsf{Q}}^{2}
with
not higgsed colors and
n_{2}<(N_{c}-n_{1})
flavors,   c)
n_{1}^{2}
pions

\Pi_{11}
,   d)
2n_{1}n_{2}
hybrid pions
\Pi_{12}
and
\Pi_{21}

(in essence, these are the quarks
{\overline{Q}}_{2},\,Q^{2}
with
higgsed colors in this case). The scale factor
{\Lambda_{1}}
of
the gauge coupling in this lower energy theory is

\displaystyle\Lambda^{{\rm b}^{\prime}_{\rm o}}_{1}\sim\Lambda_{Q}^{\rm{b_{o}}}/\det\Pi_{11}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt{\rm b}^{\prime}_{\rm o}=3(N_{c}-n_{1})-n_{2}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\rm{b_{o}}=3N_{c}-N_{F}\,.

(15.2.9)

The scale of the perturbative pole mass of
{\overline{\textsf{Q}}}_{2},\,{\textsf{Q}}^{2}

quarks is
m_{Q}^{\rm pole}\sim m_{Q}
, while the scale of

\mu_{\rm gl,2}
is
\mu_{\rm gl,2}\sim\langle{\overline{\textsf{Q}}}_{2}{\textsf{Q}}^{2}\rangle^{1/2}=\langle\Pi_{2}\rangle^{1/2}
, with
\langle\Pi_{2}\rangle\ll\langle\Pi_{1}\rangle
given in (
15.2.5
). Hence, the
hierarchy at
m_{1}\ll\mu_{\Phi}\ll{\tilde{\mu}}_{\Phi}
looks as

m_{Q}\ll{\Lambda_{1}}\ll\mu_{\rm gl,2}\sim\langle\Pi_{2}\rangle^{1/2}
and active
{\overline{\textsf{Q}}}_{2},\,{\textsf{Q}}^{2}

quarks are also higgsed, while at
\mu_{\Phi}\gg{\tilde{\mu}}_{\Phi}

the hierarchy looks as
\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle^{1/2}\ll{\Lambda_{1}}\ll m_{Q}
and the active quarks
{\overline{\textsf{Q}}}_{2},\,{\textsf{Q}}^{2}

become too heavy, they are not higgsed but are in the
\rm HQ_{2}

(heavy quark) phase. The phase changes at

\displaystyle\langle\Pi_{2}\rangle^{1/2}\sim m_{Q}\sim{\langle\Lambda_{1}\rangle}\sim{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(\rm br1)}\,\,\rightarrow\,\,{\tilde{\mu}}_{\Phi}\sim\Lambda_{Q}\Biggl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{m_{Q}}\Biggr)^{\frac{\rm{b_{o}}-n_{1}}{n_{1}}}\gg m_{1}\,.

(15.2.10)

Hence, we consider now this
Higgs_{1}-HQ_{2}
phase realized at

\mu_{\Phi}>{\tilde{\mu}}_{\Phi}
. For this it is convenient to retain
all fields
\Phi
although, in essence, they are too heavy and
dynamically irrelevant. After integrating out all heavy higgsed
gluons and
{\overline{Q}}_{1},Q^{1}
quarks, we write the Lagrangian at

\mu^{2}=\mu^{2}_{\rm gl,1}\sim N_{c}g^{2}(\mu=\mu_{\rm gl,1})z_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu_{\rm gl,1})\langle\Pi_{1}\rangle
in the form

\displaystyle K=\Bigl[\,\frac{z_{\Phi}}{f^{2}}{\rm Tr}(\Phi^{\dagger}\Phi)+z_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu^{2}_{\rm gl,1})\Bigl(K_{\Pi}+K_{{\textsf{Q}}_{2}}\Bigr)\,\Bigr],

(15.2.11)

K_{{\textsf{Q}}_{2}}={\rm Tr}\Bigl({\textsf{Q}}^{\dagger}_{2}{\textsf{Q}}^{2}+({\textsf{Q}}^{2}\rightarrow{\overline{\textsf{Q}}}_{2})\Bigr)\,,\hskip 11.74988ptK_{\Pi}=2{\rm Tr}\sqrt{\Pi^{\dagger}_{11}\Pi_{11}}+K_{\rm hybr},

K_{\rm hybr}={\rm Tr}\Biggl(\Pi^{\dagger}_{12}\frac{1}{\sqrt{\Pi_{11}\Pi^{\dagger}_{11}}}\Pi_{12}+\Pi_{21}\frac{1}{\sqrt{\Pi^{\dagger}_{11}\Pi_{11}}}\Pi^{\dagger}_{21}\Biggr),

{\cal W}=\Bigl[\frac{2\pi}{\alpha(\mu_{\rm gl,1})}{\textsf{S}}\Bigr]+\frac{\mu_{\Phi}}{2}\Biggl[{\rm Tr}\,(\Phi^{2})-\frac{1}{{\overline{N}}_{c}}\Bigl({\rm Tr}\,\Phi\Bigr)^{2}\Biggr]+{\rm Tr}\Bigl({\overline{\textsf{Q}}}_{2}m^{\rm tot}_{{\textsf{Q}}_{2}}{\textsf{Q}}^{2}\Bigr)+{\cal W}_{\Pi},

{\cal W}_{\Pi}={\rm Tr}\Bigl(m_{Q}\Pi_{11}+m^{\rm tot}_{{\textsf{Q}}_{2}}\,\Pi_{21}\frac{1}{\Pi_{11}}\Pi_{12}\Bigr)-{\rm Tr}\Bigl(\Phi_{11}\Pi_{11}+\Phi_{12}\Pi_{21}+\Phi_{21}\Pi_{12}\Bigr),\hskip 11.74988ptm^{\rm tot}_{{\textsf{Q}}_{2}}=(m_{Q}-\Phi_{22}).

In (
15.2.11
):
{\overline{\textsf{Q}}}_{2},\,\textsf{Q}^{2}
are the active
{\overline{Q}}_{2},Q^{2}
guarks with not higgsed colors (
S

is th field strength squared of not higgsed gluons),
\Pi_{12},\Pi_{21}
are the hybrid pions (in essence, these are the
{\overline{Q}}_{2},Q^{2}
guarks with higgsed colors),
z_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu^{2}_{\rm gl,1})\gg 1
is the corresponding perturbative logarithmic
renormalization factor of massless quarks, see (
15.1.2
).
Evolving now down in the scale and integrating
{\overline{\textsf{Q}}}_{2},\,\textsf{Q}^{2}

quarks as heavy ones at
\mu<m^{\rm pole}_{{\textsf{Q}}_{2}}
and then
not higgsed gluons at
\mu<{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(\rm br1)}
one obtains the
Lagrangian of pions and fions

\displaystyle K=\Bigl[\frac{z^{\rm\prime}_{\Phi}}{f^{2}}{\rm Tr}(\Phi^{\dagger}\Phi)+z_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu^{2}_{\rm gl,1})K_{\Pi}\Bigr],\,

(15.2.12)

W=(N_{c}-n_{1}){\textsf{S}}+\frac{\mu_{\Phi}}{2}\Biggl[{\rm Tr}(\Phi^{2})-\frac{1}{{\overline{N}}_{c}}\Bigl({\rm Tr}\,\Phi\Bigr)^{2}\Biggr]+W_{\Pi}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\hskip 11.74988ptS=\Biggl[\frac{\Lambda_{Q}^{\rm{b_{o}}}\det m^{\rm tot}_{{\textsf{Q}}_{2}}}{\det\Pi_{11}}\Biggr]^{\frac{1}{N_{c}-n_{1}}}\,,

We start with determining the masses of hybrids
\Pi_{12},\Pi_{21}
and
\Phi_{12},\Phi_{21}
. They are mixed and their
kinetic and mass terms look as

\displaystyle K_{\rm hybr}={\rm Tr}\Bigl[\phi^{\dagger}_{12}\phi_{12}+\phi^{\dagger}_{21}\phi_{21}+\pi^{\dagger}_{12}\pi_{12}+\pi^{\dagger}_{21}\pi_{21}\Bigr],

(15.2.13)

W_{\rm hybr}={\rm Tr}\Bigl(m_{\phi}\phi_{12}\phi_{21}+m_{\pi}\pi_{12}\pi_{21}-m_{\rm\phi\pi}(\phi_{12}\pi_{21}+\phi_{21}\pi_{12})\Bigr)\,,

m_{\phi}=\frac{f^{2}\mu_{\Phi}}{z^{\prime}_{\Phi}},\hskip 11.74988ptm_{\pi}=\frac{m_{Q}-\langle\Phi_{2}\rangle}{z_{Q}}=\frac{\langle\Pi_{1}\rangle}{\mu_{\Phi}z_{Q}}\sim\frac{m_{Q}}{z_{Q}}\ll m_{\phi}\,,\hskip 11.74988ptz_{Q}=z_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu_{\rm gl,1})\,,

\displaystyle m_{\rm\phi\pi}=\Bigl(\frac{f^{2}\langle\Pi_{1}\rangle}{z_{Q}z^{\prime}_{\Phi}}\Bigr)^{1/2},\hskip 11.74988ptm_{\rm\phi\pi}^{2}=m_{\phi}m_{\pi}\,.

(15.2.14)

Hence, the scalar potential looks as

\displaystyle V_{S}=|m|^{2}\cdot|\Psi^{(-)}_{12}|^{2}+0\cdot|\Psi^{(+)}_{12}|^{2}+(12\rightarrow 21),\hskip 11.74988pt|m|=(|m_{\phi}|+|m_{\pi}|)\,,

(15.2.15)

\Psi^{(-)}_{12}=\Bigl(c\,\phi_{12}-s\,\pi_{12}\Bigr),\hskip 11.74988pt\Psi^{(+)}_{12}=\Bigl(c\,\pi_{12}+s\,\phi_{12}\Bigr),\hskip 11.74988ptc=\Bigl(\frac{|m_{\phi}|}{|m|}\Bigr)^{1/2},\hskip 11.74988pts=\Bigl(\frac{|m_{\pi}|}{|m|}\Bigr)^{1/2}\,.

Therefore, the fields
\Psi^{(-)}_{12}
and
\Psi^{(-)}_{21}

are heavy, with the masses
|m|\approx|m_{\phi}|\gg\Lambda_{Q}
, while
the fields
\Psi^{(+)}_{12}
and
\Psi^{(+)}_{21}
are massless.
But the mixing is really parametrically small, so that the heavy
fields are mainly
\phi_{12},\phi_{21}
while the massless ones
are mainly
\pi_{12},\pi_{21}
.

33
33

33

Everywhere below in the text we neglect mixing when it is small.

And finally from (
15.2.12
), the pole mass of pions

\Pi_{11}
is

\displaystyle\mu(\Pi_{11})\sim\frac{\langle\Pi_{1}\rangle}{z_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu_{\rm gl,1})\mu_{\Phi}}\sim\frac{m_{Q}}{z_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu_{\rm gl,1})}\,.

(15.2.16)

On the whole for this
Higgs_{1}-HQ_{2}
phase the mass spectrum
looks as follows at
\mu_{\Phi}\gg{\tilde{\mu}}_{\Phi}
. a) The
heaviest are
n_{1}(2N_{c}-n_{1})
massive gluons and the same number
of their scalar superpartners with the masses
\mu_{\rm gl,1}
,
see (
15.2.6
), these masses originate from the higgsing of

{\overline{Q}}_{1},Q^{1}
quarks. b) There is a large number of
22-flavored hadrons made of weakly interacting and weakly
confined non-relativistic
{\overline{\textsf{Q}}}_{2},{\textsf{Q}}^{2}
quarks with not higgsed
colors (the tension of the confining string originated from the
unbroken
SU(N_{c}-n_{1})
color group is
\sqrt{\sigma}\sim{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(\rm br1)}\ll m^{\rm pole}_{\textsf{Q},2}
, see (
15.2.8
), the scale of
their masses is
m^{\rm pole}_{\textsf{Q},2}\sim m_{Q}/[z_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu_{\rm gl,1})z^{\prime}_{Q}(\mu_{\rm gl,1},m^{\rm pole}_{{\textsf{Q}}_{2}}]
,
where
z_{Q}\gg 1
and
z^{\rm\prime}_{Q}\ll 1
are the
corresponding massless perturbative logarithmic renormalization
factors. c) There are
n_{1}^{2}
pions
\Pi_{11}
with the masses
(
15.2.16
),
\mu(\Pi_{11})\ll m^{\rm pole}_{\textsf{Q},2}
. d)
There is a large number of gluonia made of gluons with not higgsed
colors, the scale of their masses is
\sim{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(\rm br1)}
, see
(
15.2.8
). e) The hybrids
\Pi_{12},\Pi_{21}
are
massless.

All
N^{2}_{F}
fions
\Phi_{ij}
remain too heavy and dynamically
irrelevant, their pole masses are
\mu^{\rm pole}_{1}(\Phi)\sim\mu_{\Phi}\gg\mu_{\rm gl,1}
.

16

Quark and gluino condensates and multiplicities of
vacua at
\mathbf{N_{c}<N_{F}<2N_{c}}

To obtain the numerical values of the quark
condensates
\langle{\overline{Q}}_{j}Q^{i}\rangle=\delta^{i}_{j}\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{i}\rangle
at
N_{c}<N_{F}<2N_{c}
(but only for
this purpose), the simplest way is to use the known
exact
form
of the non-perturbative contribution to the superpotential
in the standard SQCD with the quark superpotential
m_{Q}{\rm Tr}({\overline{Q}}Q)
and without the fions
\Phi
. It seems clear that
at sufficiently large values of
\mu_{\Phi}
and fixed
\Lambda_{Q}
among the vacua
of the
\Phi
-theory there should be
N_{c}
vacua of ordinary SQCD
(i.e. without fields
\Phi
) in which, definitely, all fions
\Phi
are too
heavy and dynamically irrelevant. Therefore, they all can be integrated
out and
the exact superpotential can be written as

\displaystyle{\mathcal{W}}=-{\overline{N}}_{c}\Bigl(\frac{\det{({\overline{Q}}Q)}}{\Lambda_{Q}^{\rm{b_{o}}}}\Bigr)^{1/{\overline{N}}_{c}}+m_{Q}{\rm Tr}\,{({\overline{Q}}Q)}-\frac{1}{2\mu_{\Phi}}\Biggl[{\rm Tr}\,\Bigl(({\overline{Q}}Q)^{2}\Bigr)-\frac{1}{N_{c}}\Bigl({\rm Tr}\,({\overline{Q}}Q)\Bigr)^{2}\Biggr],

(16.0.1)

where
m_{Q}=m_{Q}(\mu=\Lambda_{Q}),\,\mu_{\Phi}=\mu_{\Phi}(\mu=\Lambda_{Q})
, see sections 14 and 15 above
and sections 3 and 7 in
[
11
]
).

Indeed, at sufficiently large
\mu_{\Phi}
and fixed
\Lambda_{Q}
, there are
N_{c}
vacuum
solutions for (
16.0.1
) with the unbroken
SU(N_{F})
flavor
symmetry. In these, the last term in (
16.0.1
) gives a small
correction only and can be neglected and one obtains

\displaystyle\langle{\overline{Q}}_{j}Q^{i}\rangle_{SQCD}\approx\delta^{i}_{j}\frac{1}{m_{Q}}\Bigl({\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(\rm SQCD)}\Bigr)^{3}=\delta^{i}_{j}\frac{1}{m_{Q}}\Bigl(\Lambda_{Q}^{\rm{b_{o}}}m_{Q}^{N_{F}}\Bigr)^{1/N_{c}}\,,

(16.0.2)

as it should be.

Now, using the holomorphic dependence of the exact
superpotential on the chiral superfields
({\overline{Q}}_{j}Q^{i})
and
on chiral parameters
m_{Q}
and
\mu_{\Phi}
, the exact form
(
16.0.1
) can be used to find the values of the quark
condensates
\langle{\overline{Q}}_{j}Q^{i}\rangle
in all other vacua of
the
\Phi
- theory and at all other values of
\mu_{\Phi}>\Lambda_{Q}
. It is
worth recalling only that, in general, as in the standard SQCD

[
11
,
20
]
: (
16.0.1
)
is not the superpotential of
the genuine low energy Lagrangian describing lightest particles,
it determines only the values of the vacuum condensates

\langle{\overline{Q}}_{j}Q^{i}\rangle
. The genuine low energy
Lagrangians in different vacua will be obtained below in
sections 18-23, both in the direct and dual theories.

16.1

Vacua with the unbroken flavor symmetry

One obtains from (
16.0.1
) that at
\Lambda_{Q}\ll\mu_{\Phi}\ll m_{1}
there
are two groups of such vacua with parametrically different
values of condensates,
\langle{\overline{Q}}_{j}Q^{i}\rangle_{L}=\delta^{i}_{j}\langle{\overline{Q}}Q\rangle_{L}
and
\langle{\overline{Q}}_{j}Q^{i}\rangle_{S}=\delta^{i}_{j}\langle{\overline{Q}}Q\rangle_{S}
.

a) There are
(2N_{c}-N_{F})
L - vacua (L=large), with

\displaystyle\langle{\overline{Q}}Q(\mu=\Lambda_{Q})\rangle_{L}\sim\Lambda_{Q}^{2}\Biggl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\Biggr)^{\frac{{\overline{N}}_{c}}{2N_{c}-N_{F}}}\ll\Lambda_{Q}^{2}\,.

(16.1.1)

In these quantum L -vacua the second term in the superpotential
(
16.0.1
) gives numerically only small correction.

b) There are
(N_{F}-N_{c})
classical S - vacua (S=small)
with

\displaystyle\langle{\overline{Q}}Q(\mu=\Lambda_{Q})\rangle_{S}\approx-\frac{N_{c}}{{\overline{N}}_{c}}\,m_{Q}\mu_{\Phi}\,.

(16.1.2)

In these S - vacua, the first non-perturbative term in the
superpotential (
16.0.1
) gives only small corrections with

Z_{N_{F}-N_{c}}
phases, but just these corrections determine the
multiplicity of these
(N_{F}-N_{c})
nearly degenerate vacua. On
the whole, there are

\displaystyle N_{\rm unbrok}=(2N_{c}-N_{F})+(N_{F}-N_{c})=N_{c}

(16.1.3)

vacua with the unbroken flavor symmetry at
N_{c}<N_{F}<2N_{c}
.

One obtains from (
16.0.1
) that at
\mu_{\Phi}\gg m_{1}
the above

(2N_{c}-N_{F})
L - vacua and
(N_{F}-N_{c})
S - vacua degenerate into

N_{c}
SQCD vacua (
16.0.2
).

The value of
m_{1}
is determined from the matching

\Biggl[\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)\rangle_{L}\sim\Lambda_{Q}^{2}\Biggl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{m_{1}}\Biggr)^{\frac{{\overline{N}}_{c}}{2N_{c}-N_{F}}}\Biggr]\sim\Biggl[\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)\rangle_{S}\sim m_{Q}m_{1}\Biggl]\sim\Biggl[\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)\rangle_{\rm SQCD}\sim\Lambda_{Q}^{2}\Bigl(\frac{m_{Q}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\frac{{\overline{N}}_{c}}{N_{c}}}\Biggl]\hskip 11.74988pt\rightarrow

\displaystyle\rightarrow m_{1}\sim\Lambda_{Q}\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{m_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{N_{c}}}\gg\Lambda_{Q}\,.

(16.1.4)

16.2

Vacua with the spontaneously broken flavor symmetry

In these, there are
n_{1}
equal condensates
\langle{\overline{Q}}_{1}Q^{1}(\mu=\Lambda_{Q})\rangle\equiv\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle
and
n_{2}\geq n_{1}
equal condensates
\langle{\overline{Q}}_{2}Q^{2}(\mu=\Lambda_{Q})\rangle\equiv\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle\neq\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle
. The simplest way to find the
values of quark condensates in these vacua is to use
(
14.1.5
). We rewrite it here for convenience

\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}+({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}-\frac{1}{N_{c}}{\rm Tr}\,({\overline{Q}}Q)\rangle_{\rm br}=m_{Q}\mu_{\Phi}\,,

\displaystyle\langle S\rangle_{\rm br}=\Bigl(\frac{\det\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)\rangle_{\rm br}=\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle^{\rm{n}_{1}}_{\rm br}\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle^{\rm{n}_{2}}_{\rm br}}{\Lambda_{Q}^{\rm{b_{o}}}}\Bigr)^{1/{\overline{N}}_{c}}=\frac{\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{\rm br}\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{\rm br}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\,.

(16.2.1)

Besides, the multiplicity of vacua will be shown below at given

\displaystyle N_{\rm brok}(n_{1})=\Bigl[(N_{c}-n_{1})+\theta(N_{c}-n_{2})(N_{c}-n_{2})\Bigr]{\overline{C}}^{\,n_{1}}_{N_{F}}=

vacua. As it should be, the number of vacua at

As one can see from the above, all quark condensates become
parametrically the same at
\mu_{\Phi}\sim m_{1}
. Clearly, this region

\mu_{\Phi}\sim m_{1}
is very special and most of the quark condensates
change their parametric behavior and hierarchies at

\mu_{\Phi}\lessgtr m_{1}
. For example, the br2 - vacua with

n_{2}<N_{c}\,,\,\,\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle\sim m_{Q}\mu_{\Phi}\gg\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle
at
\mu_{\Phi}\gg m_{1}
evolve into the L -
type vacua with
\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle\sim\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle\sim\Lambda_{Q}^{2}(\Lambda_{Q}/\mu_{\Phi})^{{\overline{N}}_{c}/(2N_{c}-N_{F})}
at
\mu_{\Phi}\ll m_{1}
, while the br2 -

at
\mu_{\Phi}\ll m_{1}
evolve into the br1
- vacua with
\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle\sim m_{Q}\mu_{\Phi}\gg\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle

at
\mu_{\Phi}\gg m_{1}
, etc.
The exception is the special vacua with

n_{1}={\overline{N}}_{c},\,n_{2}=N_{c}
. In these, the parametric behavior

\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle\sim m_{Q}\mu_{\Phi},\,\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle\sim\Lambda_{Q}^{2}(\Lambda_{Q}/\mu_{\Phi})^{{\overline{N}}_{c}/(2N_{c}-N_{F})}
remains the same but the
hierarchy is reversed at
\mu_{\Phi}\lessgtr m_{1}\,:\,\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle/\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle\sim(\mu_{\Phi}/m_{1})^{N_{c}/(2N_{c}-N_{F})}
.

The total number of all vacua at
N_{c}<N_{F}<2N_{c}
is

\displaystyle N_{\rm tot}=\Bigl(N_{\rm unbrok}=N_{c}\Bigr)+\Bigl(N_{\rm brok}^{\rm tot}=\sum_{n_{1}=1}^{[N_{F}/2]}N_{\rm brok}(n_{1})\Bigr)=\sum_{k=0}^{N_{c}}(N_{c}-k)C^{\,k}_{N_{F}}\,,

(16.2.9)

this agrees with

But we disagree with their ‘derivation’ in their section 4.3. There is

\langle\det M/\Lambda_{Q}^{\rm{b_{o}}}\rangle^{1/{\overline{N}}_{c}}=\langle\det{\overline{Q}}Q/\Lambda_{Q}^{\rm{b_{o}}}\rangle^{1/{\overline{N}}_{c}}=\langle S\rangle\neq 0
at

(see sections 18-23 below ). Really, the

Comparing this with the number of vacua

The analog of (
16.0.1
) in the dual theory with

\Lambda_{Q}=-\Lambda_{q}
is obtained by the
replacement
{\overline{Q}}Q(\mu=\Lambda_{Q})\rightarrow M(\mu=\Lambda_{Q})
, so that
\langle M(\mu=\Lambda_{Q})\rangle=\langle{\overline{Q}}Q(\mu=\Lambda_{Q})\rangle
in all
vacua and multiplicities of vacua are the same.

17

Fions
\mathbf{\Phi}
in the direct theory : one or
three generations

At
N_{c}<N_{F}<2N_{c}
and in the interval of scales

\mu_{H}<\mu<\Lambda_{Q}
(
\mu_{H}
is the largest physical mass in the
quark-gluon sector), the quark and gluon fields are effectively
massless. Because the quark renormalization factor
z_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu\ll\Lambda_{Q})=(\mu/\Lambda_{Q})^{\gamma_{Q}>\,0}\ll 1
in the Kahler term
decreases in
this case in a
power fashion
with lowering energy due to
the perturbative RG evolution, it is seen from (
14.1.3
)
that the role of the 4-quark term
({\overline{Q}}Q)^{2}/\mu_{\Phi}
increases
with lowering energy. Hence, while it is irrelevant at the scale

\mu\sim\Lambda_{Q}
because
\mu_{\Phi}\gg\Lambda_{Q}
, the question is whether it
becomes dynamically relevant in the range of energies

\mu_{H}\ll\mu\ll\Lambda_{Q}
. For this, we estimate the scale
\mu_{o}

where it becomes relevant in the massless theory (see section 7
in
[
11
]
for the perturbative strong coupling regime with

a(\mu\sim\Lambda_{Q})\sim 1,\,a(\mu\ll\Lambda_{Q})\sim(\Lambda_{Q}/\mu)^{\nu\,>\,0}\gg 1
at
N_{c}<N_{F}<3N_{c}/2
)

\displaystyle\frac{\mu_{o}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\frac{1}{z^{2}_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu_{o})}=\frac{\mu_{o}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{\mu_{o}}\Bigr)^{2\gamma_{Q}}\sim 1\hskip 11.74988pt\rightarrow\hskip 11.74988pt\frac{\mu_{o}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\sim\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\Bigr)^{\frac{1}{(2\gamma_{Q}-1)}}\,\,,

(17.0.1)

\gamma^{\rm conf}_{Q}=\frac{\rm{b_{o}}}{N_{F}}\,\rightarrow\,\frac{\mu^{\rm conf}_{o}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\sim\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\Bigr)^{\frac{N_{F}}{3(2N_{c}-N_{F})}}\,\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\gamma^{\rm strong}_{Q}=\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{{\overline{N}}_{c}}\,\rightarrow\,\frac{\mu^{\rm strong}_{o}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\sim\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\Bigr)^{\frac{{\overline{N}}_{c}}{(5N_{c}-3N_{F})}}\,\,.

Hence, if
\mu_{H}\ll\mu_{o}
, then at scales
\mu<\mu_{o}
the
four-quark terms in the superpotential (
14.1.3
) cannot be
neglected any more and we have to account for them. For this, we
have to reinstate the fion fields
\Phi
and to use the
Lagrangian (
14.1.1
) in which the Kahler term at

\mu_{H}<\mu\ll\Lambda_{Q}
looks as

\displaystyle K=\Bigl[z_{\Phi}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu){\rm Tr}\,(\Phi^{\dagger}\Phi)+z_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu){\rm Tr}\Bigl(Q^{\dagger}Q+(Q\rightarrow{\overline{Q}})\Bigr)\Bigr],\,\,z_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu)=\Bigl(\frac{\mu}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\gamma_{Q}}\ll 1.

(17.0.2)

We recall that even at those scales
\mu
that the running
perturbative mass of fions
\mu_{\Phi}(\mu)\equiv\mu_{\Phi}/z_{\Phi}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu)\gg\mu
and so they are too heavy and
dynamically irrelevant, the quarks and gluons remain effectively
massless and active. Therefore, due to the Yukawa interactions
of fions with quarks, the loops of still active light quarks
(and gluons interacting with quarks) still induce the running
renormalization factor
z_{\Phi}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu)
of fions at all those
scales until quarks are effectively massless,
\mu>\mu_{H}
. But,
in contrast with a very slow logarithmic RG evolution at

N_{F}<N_{c}
in section 15, the perturbative running mass of fions
decreases now at
N_{c}<N_{F}<2N_{c}
and
\mu<\Lambda_{Q}
monotonically
in a power fashin with diminishing scale (see below),

\mu_{\Phi}(\mu\ll\Lambda_{Q})=\mu_{\Phi}/z_{\Phi}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu)\sim\mu_{\Phi}(\mu/\Lambda_{Q})^{|\gamma_{\Phi}|>1}\ll\mu_{\Phi}
.
Nevertheless, until
\mu_{\Phi}(\mu)\gg\mu
, the fields
\Phi
remain
heavy and do not influence the RG evolution. But, when

\mu_{H}\ll\mu_{o}
and
\mu_{\Phi}(\mu)\sim\mu_{\Phi}/z_{\Phi}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu)
is the
main contribution to the fion mass

35
35

35

the cases when the additional contributions to the masses of
fions from other perturbative or non-perturbative terms in the
superpotential are not small in comparison with

\sim\mu_{\Phi}/z_{\Phi}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu)
have to be considered
separately

,
the quickly decreasing mass
\mu_{\Phi}(\mu)
becomes
\mu^{\rm pole}_{2}(\Phi)=\mu_{\Phi}(\mu=\mu^{\rm pole}_{2}(\Phi))
and

\mu_{\Phi}(\mu<\mu^{\rm pole}_{2}(\Phi))<\mu
, so that : 1) there is
a pole in the fion propagator at
p=\mu^{\rm pole}_{2}(\Phi)

(ignoring here and below a nonzero fion width, in any case the
nonzero width can have only massive particle), this is a second
generation of fions (the first one is at
\mu^{\rm pole}_{1}(\Phi)\gg\Lambda_{Q}
; 2) the fields
\Phi

become effectively massless at
\mu<\mu^{\rm pole}_{2}(\Phi)

and begins to influence the perturbative RG evolution
. In other
words, the seemingly ‘heavy’ fields
\Phi

return back
,
they become effectively massless and dynamically
relevant
.
Here and below the terms ‘relevant’ and ‘irrelevant’ (at a given
scale
\mu
) will be used in the sense of whether the running
mass
\sim\mu_{\Phi}/z_{\Phi}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu\ll\Lambda_{Q})
of fions at a given
scale
\mu
is
<\mu
, so that they are effectively massless and
participate actively in interactions at this scale , or they
remain too heavy with the running mass
>\mu
whose interactions
at this scale give only small corrections.

It seems clear that, at
N_{c}<N_{F}<2N_{c}
,
the physical reason why
the
4
-quark
terms in the superpotential (
14.1.3
) become relevant at
scales
\mu<\mu_{o}
is that the fion field
\Phi
which was too
heavy and so dynamically irrelevant at
\mu>\mu_{o},\,\mu_{\Phi}(\mu>\mu_{o})>\mu
, becomes effectively massless at

\mu<\mu_{o},\,\mu_{\Phi}(\mu<\mu_{o})<\mu
, and begins to participate
actively in the RG evolution, i.e. it becomes relevant
. In
other words, the four quark term in (
14.1.3
) ’remembers’
about fions and signals about the scale below which the fions
become effectively massless,
\mu_{o}=\mu^{\rm pole}_{2}(\Phi)
.
This allows us to find the value of
z_{\Phi}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu_{o})
,
see (
17.0.1
),

\frac{\mu_{\Phi}}{z_{\Phi}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu_{o})}\approx\mu_{o}\,,\hskip 11.74988ptz_{\Phi}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu_{o}<\mu\ll\Lambda_{Q})=\Bigl(\frac{\mu}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\gamma_{\Phi}}\,,

\displaystyle\gamma_{\Phi}=-2\gamma_{Q}\,<\,0\,,\hskip 11.74988ptf^{2}=f^{2}(\mu=\Lambda_{Q})=1\,.

(17.0.3)

The perturbative running mass

\mu_{\Phi}(\mu)=\mu_{\Phi}/z_{\Phi}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu\ll\Lambda_{Q})\ll\mu_{\Phi}
of fions
continues to decrease strongly with diminishing
\mu
at all
scales
\mu_{H}<\mu<\Lambda_{Q}
until quarks remain effectively massless,
and becomes frozen only at scales below the quark physical mass,
when the heavy quarks decouple.

Hence, if
\mu_{H}\gg\mu_{o}
, there is no pole in the fion
propagator at momenta
p<\Lambda_{Q}
because the running fion mass is
too large in this range of scales,
\mu_{\Phi}(p>\mu_{o})>p
. The fions
remain dynamically irrelevant in this case at all momenta

p<\Lambda_{Q}
.

But when
\mu_{H}\ll\mu_{o}
,
there will be not only the second
generation of fions at
p=\mu^{\rm pole}_{2}(\Phi)=\mu_{o}
but also
a third generation at
p\ll\mu_{o}
. Indeed, after the heavy
quarks decouple at momenta
p<\mu_{H}\ll\mu_{o}
and the
renormalization factor
z_{\Phi}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu)
of fions becomes
frozen in the region of scales where the fions already became
relevant,
z_{\Phi}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu<\mu_{H})\sim z_{\Phi}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu\sim\mu_{H})
, the frozen value
\mu_{\Phi}(\mu<\mu_{H})
of the running
perturbative fion mass is now
\mu_{\Phi}(\mu\sim\mu_{H})\ll p_{H}=\mu_{H}
.
Hence, there is one more pole in the fion propagator at

p=\mu^{\rm pole}_{3}(\Phi)\sim\mu_{\Phi}(\mu\sim\mu_{H})\ll\mu_{H}
.

On the whole, in a few words for the direct theory (see the
footnote
35
for reservations).

a) The fions remain dynamically irrelevant and there are
no poles in the fion propagator at momenta
p<\Lambda_{Q}
if

\mu_{H}\gg\mu_{o}
.

b) If
\mu_{H}\ll\mu_{o}\ll\Lambda_{Q}
, there are two poles in the
fion propagator at momenta
p\ll\Lambda_{Q}
:
\mu^{\rm pole}_{2}(\Phi)\sim\mu_{o}
and
\mu^{\rm pole}_{3}(\Phi)\sim\mu_{\Phi}/z_{\Phi}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu_{H})\ll\mu^{\rm pole}_{2}(\Phi)
(here and
everywhere below in similar cases, - up to corrections due to
nonzero decay widths of fions). In other words, the
fions appear in three generations in this case (we recall that
there is always the largest pole mass of fions
\mu^{\rm pole}_{1}(\Phi)\gg\Lambda_{Q}
). Hence, the fions are
effectively massless and dynamically relevant in the range of
scales
\mu^{\rm pole}_{3}(\Phi)<\mu<\mu^{\rm pole}_{2}(\Phi)
.

Moreover, once the fions become effectively massless and
dynamically relevant with respect to internal interactions, they
begin to contribute simultaneously to the external anomalies (
the ’t Hooft triangles in the external background fields).

The case
\mu_{H}\sim\mu_{o}
requires additional information. The
reason is that at scales
\mu\lesssim\mu_{H}
, in addition to the
canonical kinetic term
\Phi^{\dagger}_{R}p^{2}\Phi_{R}

(R=renormalized) of fions, there are also terms
\sim\Phi^{\dagger}_{R}p^{2}(p^{2}/\mu_{H}^{2})^{k}\Phi_{R}
with higher powers of
momenta induced by loops of heavy quarks (and gluons). If

\mu_{H}\ll\mu_{o}
, then the pole in the fion propagator at

p=\mu^{\rm pole}_{2}(\Phi)=\mu_{o}
is definitely there and,
because
\mu_{\Phi}(\mu=\mu_{H})\ll\mu_{H}
, these additional terms are
irrelevant in the region
p\sim\mu_{\Phi}(\mu=\mu_{H})\ll\mu_{H}
and the
pole in the fion propagator at
p=\mu^{\rm pole}_{3}(\Phi)=\mu_{\Phi}(\mu=\mu_{H})\ll\mu_{H}
is also guaranteed. But

\mu_{\Phi}(\mu\sim\mu_{H})\sim\mu_{H}
if
\mu_{H}\sim\mu_{o}
, and these
additional terms become relevant. Hence, whether there is a pole
in the fion propagator in this case or not depends on all these
terms.

Now, if
\mu_{H}<\mu_{o}
so that the fions become relevant at

\mu<\mu_{o}
, the question is : what are the values of the quark
and fion anomalous dimensions,
\gamma_{Q}
and
\gamma_{\Phi}
, in
the massless perturbative regime at
\mu_{H}<\mu<\mu_{o}
?

To answer this question, we use the approach used in
[
11
]

(see section 7 therein). For this, we introduce first the corresponding
massless Seiberg dual theory
[
5
]
. Our direct theory
includes at
\mu_{H}<\mu<\mu^{\rm conf}_{o}
not only the original
effectively massless in this range of scales quark and gluon
fields, but also the active
N_{F}^{2}
fion fields
\Phi^{j}_{i}
as
they became now also effectively massless, so that the effective
superpotential becomes nonzero and includes the Yukawa term

{\rm Tr}\,({\overline{Q}}\Phi Q)
. Then, the massless dual theory with
the same ’t Hooft triangles includes only the massless qual
quarks
{\overline{q}},\,q
with
N_{F}
flavors and the dual

SU({\overline{N}}_{c}=N_{F}-N_{c})
gluons. Further, one equates two NSVZ

\,{\widehat{\beta}}_{ext}
- functions of the external baryon and

SU(N_{F})_{L,R}
- flavor vector fields in the direct and dual
theories,

\displaystyle\frac{d}{d\,\ln\mu}\,\frac{2\pi}{\alpha_{ext}}={\widehat{\beta}}_{ext}=-\frac{2\pi}{\alpha^{2}_{ext}}\,\beta_{ext}=\sum_{i}T_{i}\,\bigl(1+\gamma_{i}\bigr)\,,

(17.0.4)

where the sum runs over all fields which are effectively
massless at scales
\mu_{H}<\mu<\mu_{o}
, the unity in the brackets
is due to one-loop contributions while the anomalous dimensions

\gamma_{i}
of fields represent all higher-loop effects,
T_{i}

are the coefficients. It is worth noting that these general NSVZ
forms (
17.0.4
) of the external ’flavored’

\widehat{\beta}
-functions are independent of the kind of
massless perturbative regime of the internal gauge theory, i.e.
whether it is conformal, or the strong coupling or the IR free
one.

The effectively massless particles in the direct theory here are
the original quarks
Q,\,{\overline{Q}}
and gluons and, in addition,
the fions
\Phi^{j}_{i}
, while in the dual theory these are the
dual quarks
q,\,{\overline{q}}
and dual gluons only.

It is clear that, in comparison with the standard SQCD without
the fion fields (see section 7 in
[
11
]
), the addition of
the fion fields with zero baryon charge does not influence

{\widehat{\beta}}_{ext}
for the baryon charge, so that in the
whole interval
\mu_{H}<\mu<\Lambda_{Q}
it remains the same as in

[
11
]

\displaystyle N_{F}N_{c}\,\Bigl(B_{Q}=1\Bigr)^{2}\,(1+\gamma_{Q})=N_{F}{\overline{N}}_{c}\,\Bigl(B_{q}=\frac{N_{c}}{{\overline{N}}_{c}}\Bigr)^{2}\,(1+\gamma_{q})\,.

(17.0.5)

The form of (
17.0.4
) for the
SU(N_{F})_{L}
flavor charge at
scales
\mu_{H}<\mu<\mu_{o}
where the fion fields became
effectively massless and relevant differs from those in

[
11
]
, now it looks as

\displaystyle N_{c}\,(1+\gamma_{Q})+N_{F}\,(1+\gamma_{\Phi})={\overline{N}}_{c}\,(1+\gamma_{q})\,.

(17.0.6)

In (
17.0.5
),(
17.0.6
): the left-hand sides are from the
direct theory while the right-hand sides are from the dual one,

\gamma_{Q}
and
\gamma_{\Phi}
are the anomalous dimensions of the
quark
Q
and fion
\Phi
, while
\gamma_{q}
is the anomalous
dimension of the dual quark
q
.

The massless dual theory is in the conformal regime at

3N_{c}/2<N_{F}<2N_{c}
, so that
\gamma^{\rm conf}_{q}=\rm{{\overline{b}}_{o}}/N_{F}=(3{\overline{N}}_{c}-N_{F})/N_{F}
. Therefore, one obtains from
(
17.0.5
),(
17.0.6
) that
\gamma^{\rm conf}_{Q}=\rm{b_{o}}/N_{F}=(3N_{c}-N_{F})/N_{F}
and
\gamma^{\rm conf}_{\Phi}=-2\gamma^{\rm conf}_{Q}
, i.e. while only the
quark-gluon sector of the direct theory behaves conformally at
scales
\mu^{\rm conf}_{o}<\mu<\Lambda_{Q}
where the fion fields
\Phi

remain heavy and irrelevant, the whole theory including the
fields
\Phi
becomes conformal at scales
\mu_{H}<\mu<\mu^{\rm conf}_{o}
where fions become effectively massless and relevant.

36
36

36

This does not mean that nothing changes at all after the fion
field
\Phi
begins to participate actively in the perturbative
RG evolution at
\mu_{H}<\mu<\mu^{\rm conf}_{o}
. In particular, the
frozen fixed point values of the gauge and Yukawa couplings

a^{*}
and
a_{f}^{*}
will change.

In the region
N_{c}<N_{F}<3N_{c}/2
the situation with
(
17.0.5
),(
17.0.6
) is somewhat different. The massless
direct theory is now in the strong gauge coupling regime
starting from
\mu<\Lambda_{Q}
,
a(\mu\ll\Lambda_{Q})\sim(\Lambda_{Q}/\mu)^{\nu\,>\,0}\gg 1
, see section 7 in
[
11
]
and
[
20
]
,
while the massless dual theory is in the IR free logarithmic regime.

Therefore,
\gamma_{q}
is logarithmically small at
\mu\ll\Lambda_{Q},\,\gamma_{q}\rightarrow 0
, and one obtains in this case from (
17.0.5
)
for the baryon charge the same value of
\gamma^{\rm strong}_{Q}(\mu_{H}\ll\mu\ll\Lambda_{Q})
as in
[
11
]

\displaystyle\gamma^{\rm strong}_{Q}(\mu_{H}\ll\mu\ll\Lambda_{Q})=\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{{\overline{N}}_{c}}\,,\hskip 11.74988pta(\mu_{H}\ll\mu\ll\Lambda_{Q})\sim(\Lambda_{Q}/\mu)^{\nu}\gg 1\,,

(17.0.7)

\displaystyle\hskip 11.74988pt\nu=\frac{N_{F}}{N_{c}}\gamma^{\rm strong}_{Q}-3=\frac{3N_{c}-2N_{F}}{{\overline{N}}_{c}}>0\,.

(17.0.8)

In other words, the value of the quark anomalous dimension
\gamma^{\rm strong}_{Q}(\mu_{H}\ll\mu\ll\Lambda_{Q})
in the
\Phi
-theory is the same as in the
standard SQCD, independently of whether the field
\Phi
is relevant or not.

Additional details can be found in
[
26
]
(see also Appendix 1).

Mass spectra at
\mathbf{3N_{c}/2<N_{F}<2N_{c}}

18

Direct theory. Unbroken flavor symmetry

18.1

L - vacua

The theory entered the conformal regime at the scale

\mu<\Lambda_{Q}
. In these
(2N_{c}-N_{F})
vacua with the unbroken flavor symmetry

U(N_{F})
the current quark mass at
\Lambda_{Q}\ll\mu_{\Phi}\ll m_{1}
looks as, see
(
16.1.1
),(
18.1.1
)

\displaystyle\langle m^{\rm tot}_{Q}\rangle_{L}\equiv\langle m^{\rm tot}_{Q}(\mu=\Lambda_{Q})\rangle_{L}=m_{Q}-\langle\Phi\rangle_{L}=m_{Q}+\frac{{\overline{N}}_{c}}{N_{c}}\frac{({\overline{Q}}Q)_{L}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\,,

(18.1.1)

({\overline{Q}}Q)_{L}\sim\Lambda_{Q}^{2}\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\Bigr)^{\frac{{\overline{N}}_{c}}{2N_{c}-N_{F}}}\gg m_{Q}\mu_{\Phi},\hskip 11.74988pt\langle m^{\rm tot}_{Q}\rangle_{L}\sim\Lambda_{Q}\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\Bigr)^{\frac{N_{c}}{2N_{c}-N_{F}}}\,,

m^{\rm pole}_{Q,\,L}=\frac{\langle m^{\rm tot}_{Q}\rangle_{L}}{z_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q},m^{\rm pole}_{Q,\,L})}\sim\Lambda_{Q}\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\Bigr)^{\frac{N_{F}}{3(2N_{c}-N_{F})}}\sim{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(L)}\,,\hskip 11.74988ptz_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu\ll\Lambda_{Q})\sim\Bigl(\frac{\mu}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\rm{b_{o}}/N_{F}}\ll 1\,.

We compare
m^{\rm pole}_{Q,\,L}
with the gluon mass due to possible higgsing of quarks.
This last looks as

\displaystyle\mu_{{\rm gl},\,L}^{2}\sim\Bigl(a_{*}\sim 1\Bigr)z_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu_{{\rm gl},\,L})({\overline{Q}}Q)_{L}\hskip 11.74988pt\rightarrow\hskip 11.74988pt\mu_{{\rm gl},\,L}\sim m^{\rm pole}_{Q,\,L}\sim{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(L)}=\langle S\rangle^{1/3}_{L}\,.

(18.1.2)

Hence, qualitatively, the situation is the same as in the standard SQCD

[
20
]
. And one can use here the same reasonings, see the footnote

5
or footnote 3 in 3 in
[
20
]
.
In the case considered, there are only
(2N_{c}-N_{F})
these isolated
L-vacua with
unbroken flavor symmetry
. If quarks were higgsed in these
L-vacua, then
the flavor symmetry will be necessary broken
spontaneously
due to the rank restriction because
N_{F}>N_{c}
.
There will appear genuine exactly massless Nambu-Goldstone fields

\Pi
(pions), so that there will be a continuous family of non-isolated
vacua. And besides, this will be in contradiction with the Konishi
anomaly which predicts equal values of
\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)\rangle_{L}

for all flavors in these L-vacua. So, we conclude that quarks are not
higgsed but are in the HQ (heavy quark) phase and are confined.

Therefore (see sections 3, 4 in
[
20
]
), after integrating out all quarks
as   heavy ones at
\mu<m^{\rm pole}_{Q,\,L}
and then all
SU(N_{c})
gluons at

\mu<{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(L)}=m^{\rm pole}_{Q,\,L}/(\rm several)
via the Veneziano-Yankielowicz (VY)
procedure
[
15
]
, we obtain the Lagrangian of fions

\displaystyle K=z_{\Phi}(\Lambda_{Q},m^{\rm pole}_{Q,\,L}){\rm Tr}\,(\Phi^{\dagger}\Phi)\,,\hskip 11.74988ptz_{\Phi}(\Lambda_{Q},m^{\rm pole}_{Q,\,L})\sim\frac{1}{z^{2}_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q},m^{\rm pole}_{Q,\,L})}\sim\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{m^{\rm pole}_{Q,\,L}}\Bigr)^{2\rm{b_{o}}/N_{F}}\gg 1\,,

(18.1.3)

{\cal W}=N_{c}S+\frac{\mu_{\Phi}}{2}\Biggl[{\rm Tr}\,(\Phi^{2})-\frac{1}{{\overline{N}}_{c}}\Bigl({\rm Tr}\,\Phi\Bigr)^{2}\Biggr]\,,\hskip 11.74988ptS=\Bigl(\Lambda_{Q}^{\rm{b_{o}}}\det m^{\rm tot}_{Q}\Bigr)^{1/N_{c}}\,,\hskip 11.74988ptm^{\rm tot}_{Q}=(m_{Q}-\Phi)\,,

and one has to choose the L - vacua in (
18.1.3
).

There are two contributions to the mass of fions in (
18.1.3
), the
perturbative one from the term
\sim\mu_{\Phi}\Phi^{2}
and the non-perturbative one
from
\sim S
, and both are parametrically the same,
\sim{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(L)}\gg m_{Q}
.
Therefore,

\displaystyle\mu(\Phi)\sim\frac{\mu_{\Phi}}{z_{\Phi}(\Lambda_{Q},m^{\rm pole}_{Q,\,L})}\sim m^{\rm pole}_{Q,\,L}\sim{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(L)}\,.

(18.1.4)

Besides, see (
17.0.1
), because

\displaystyle\mu^{\rm conf}_{o}\sim\Lambda_{Q}\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\Bigr)^{\frac{N_{F}}{3(2N_{c}-N_{F})}}\sim m^{\rm pole}_{Q,\,L}\sim{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(L)}\,,

(18.1.5)

and fions are dynamically irrelevant at
\mu^{\rm conf}_{o}<\mu<\Lambda_{Q}
and can
become relevant only at the scale
\mu<\mu^{\rm conf}_{o}
, it remains unclear in
these L - vacua whether there is a pole in the fion propagators at

p\sim\mu^{\rm conf}_{o}\sim m^{\rm pole}_{Q,\,L}
. May be yes but maybe not.

On the whole for the mass spectrum in these L - vacua. The quarks
{\overline{Q}},Q

are confined and strongly coupled here, the coupling being
a_{*}\sim 1
.
Parametrically, there is only one scale
\sim{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(L)}
in the mass spectrum
at
\Lambda_{Q}\ll\mu_{\Phi}\ll m_{1}
. And there is no parametrical guaranty that there is the
second generation of fions with the pole masses
\mu_{2}^{\rm pole}(\Phi)\sim{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(L)}
.

The condensate
\langle{\overline{Q}}Q\rangle_{L}
and the quark pole mass
m^{\rm pole}_{Q,\,L}
become
frozen at their SQCD values at
\mu_{\Phi}\gg m_{1},\,\langle{\overline{Q}}Q\rangle_{SQCD}\sim\Lambda_{Q}^{2}(m_{Q}/\Lambda_{Q})^{{\overline{N}}_{c}/N_{c}},\,\,m^{\rm pole}_{SQCD}\sim{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(SQCD)}\sim\Lambda_{Q}(m_{Q}/\Lambda_{Q})^{N_{F}/3N_{c}}

[
20
]
, while
\mu_{\Phi}
increases
and
\mu^{\rm conf}_{o}\ll m^{\rm pole}_{Q,SQCD}
decreases, see
(
17.0.1
).
Hence, the perturbative contribution
\sim\mu_{\Phi}/z_{\Phi}(\Lambda_{Q},m^{\rm pole}_{Q,\,L})\gg m^{\rm pole}_{Q,SQCD}
to the fion mass becomes dominant at
\mu_{\Phi}\gg m_{1}
and the fion
fields will be dynamically irrelevant at
\mu<\Lambda_{Q}
.

Finally, it is worth emphasizing for all what follows that, unlike the dual
theory,
in all vacua of the direct theory the mass spectra remain
parametrically the same at
\,\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}/N_{F}=O(1)
or
\,\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}/N_{F}\ll 1
.

18.2

S - vacua

In these
{\overline{N}}_{c}
vacua the quark mass at
\Lambda_{Q}\ll\mu_{\Phi}\ll m_{1}
looks as, see
(
16.1.2
),

\frac{\langle m^{\rm tot}_{Q}(\mu=\Lambda_{Q})\rangle_{S}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\sim\frac{\langle S\rangle_{S}}{\Lambda_{Q}\langle{\overline{Q}}Q\rangle_{S}}\sim\Bigl(\frac{\langle{\overline{Q}}Q\rangle_{S}}{\Lambda_{Q}^{2}}\Bigr)^{N_{c}/{\overline{N}}_{c}}\sim\Bigl(\frac{m_{Q}\mu_{\Phi}}{\Lambda_{Q}^{2}}\Bigr)^{N_{c}/{\overline{N}}_{c}}\,,

This has to be compared with the gluon mass due to possible higgsing of quarks

\displaystyle\mu_{{\rm gl},\,S}^{2}\sim z_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu_{{\rm gl},\,S})\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{S}\rangle\rightarrow\mu_{{\rm gl},\,S}\sim m^{\rm pole}_{Q,S}\sim{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(S)},\,\,z_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu_{{\rm gl},\,S})\sim\Bigl(\frac{\mu_{{\rm gl},\,S}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)_{.}^{\frac{\rm{b_{o}}}{N_{F}}}\,\,\,

(18.2.1)

For the same reasons as in previous section, it is clear that quarks will not
be  higgsed in these vacua at
N_{F}>N_{c}
(as otherwise the flavor symmetry will
be ], broken spontaneously). Hence, as in
[
20
]
, we conclude here also that
the
pole mass of quarks is the largest physical mass, i.e.
\mu_{H}=m^{\rm pole}_{Q,S}=(\rm several)\mu_{{\rm gl},\,S}
.

But, in contrast with the L - vacua, the fion fields
become dynamically
relevant in these S - vacua
at scales
\mu<\mu^{\rm conf}_{o}
, see
(
18.2.1
), if

\displaystyle\mu^{\rm conf}_{o}\sim\Lambda_{Q}\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\Bigr)^{\frac{N_{f}}{3(2N_{c}-N_{F})}}\gg m^{\rm pole}_{Q,S}\hskip 11.74988pt\rightarrow\hskip 11.74988pt{at}\hskip 11.74988pt\Lambda_{Q}\ll\mu_{\Phi}\ll m_{1}\,.

(18.2.2)

Therefore, there is the second generation of
N_{F}^{2}
fions with the pole masses

\displaystyle\mu_{2}^{\rm pole}(\Phi)\sim\mu_{o}^{\rm conf}\gg m^{\rm pole}_{Q,S}\sim{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(S)}\,.

(18.2.3)

Nevertheless, see section 17, the theory remains in the conformal regime and
quark and fion anomalous dimensions remain the same in the whole range of

m^{\rm pole}_{Q,S}<\mu<\Lambda_{Q}
of scales, but fions become effectively massless
at
\mu<\mu^{\rm conf}_{o}
and begin to contribute to the ’t Hooft triangles.

The RG evolution of the quark and fion fields becomes frozen at scales

\mu<m^{\rm pole}_{Q,S}
because the heavy quarks decouple. Proceeding as
before, i.e. integrating out first all quarks as heavy ones at
\mu<m^{\rm pole}_{Q,S}=(\rm several){\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(S)}
and then all
SU(N_{c})
gluons at

\mu<{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(S)}
,
one obtains the Lagrangian of fions as in (
18.1.3
), with a replacement

z_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q},m^{\rm pole}_{Q,\,L})\rightarrow\,z_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q},m^{\rm pole}_{Q,S})
(and the S-vacua have to be chosen
therein).

Because fions became relevant at
m^{\rm pole}_{Q,S}\ll\mu\ll\mu^{\rm conf}_{o}
,
one
could expect that their running mass will be much smaller than
m^{\rm pole}_{Q,S}
.
This is right, but only for
\mu^{\rm pert}_{\Phi}\sim\mu_{\Phi}/z_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q},m^{\rm pole}_{Q,S})\ll m^{\rm pole}_{Q,S}
. But there  is   also additional
non-perturbative

contribution
to the fion mass originating from   the region of scales
\mu\sim m^{\rm pole}_{Q,S}

and it is dominant in these S - vacua,

\displaystyle\mu(\Phi)\sim\frac{1}{z_{\Phi}(\Lambda_{Q},m^{\rm pole}_{Q,S})}\,\frac{\langle S\rangle_{S}}{\langle m^{\rm tot}_{Q}\rangle^{2}_{S}}\sim m^{\rm pole}_{Q,S}\,,\hskip 11.74988ptz_{\Phi}(\Lambda_{Q},m^{\rm pole}_{Q,S})\sim\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{m^{\rm pole}_{Q,S}}\Bigr)^{2\rm{b_{o}}/N_{F}}\,.

(18.2.4)

Therefore, despite the fact that the fions are definitely dynamically relevant
in the range of scales
m^{\rm pole}_{Q,S}\ll\mu\ll\mu^{\rm conf}_{o}\ll\Lambda_{Q}
at

\Lambda_{Q}\ll\mu_{\Phi}\ll m_{1}
, whether there is
the third generation of fions, i.e. whether   there is a pole in the fion
propagator at
p=\mu_{3}^{\rm pole}(\Phi)\sim m^{\rm pole}_{Q,S}\sim{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(S)}
remains unclear.

On the whole for the mass spectra in these S - vacua. The largest are
masses  of the second generation fions,
\mu_{2}^{\rm pole}(\Phi)\sim\Lambda_{Q}\Bigl(\Lambda_{Q}/\mu_{\Phi}\Bigr)^{N_{F}/3(2N_{c}-N_{F})}\gg m^{\rm pole}_{Q,S}
. The scale of all
other masses is
\sim m^{\rm pole}_{Q,S}\sim{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(S)}
. There is no parametrical
guaranty that there is the third generation of fions with the pole masses

\mu_{3}^{\rm pole}(\Phi)\sim{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(S)}
. May be yes, but maybe not.

The vacuum condensates
\langle{\overline{Q}}Q\rangle_{S}
and
m^{\rm pole}_{Q,S}

evolve into
their   independent of
\mu_{\Phi}
SQCD-values at
\mu_{\Phi}\gg m_{1}
,

\displaystyle\langle{\overline{Q}}Q\rangle_{SQCD}\sim\Lambda_{Q}^{2}\Bigl(\frac{m_{Q}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{{\overline{N}}_{c}/N_{c}}\,,\hskip 11.74988ptm^{\rm pole}_{Q,SQCD}\sim\Lambda_{Q}\Bigl(\frac{m_{Q}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{N_{F}/3N_{c}}\,,

(18.2.5)

and the perturbative contribution
\sim\mu_{\Phi}/z_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q},m^{\rm pole}_{Q,SQCD})
to
the fion mass becomes dominant. Hence, because
m^{\rm pole}_{Q,SQCD}\gg\mu^{\rm conf}_{o}
, the fions fields become dynamically irrelevant
at all scales
\mu<\Lambda_{Q}
when
\mu_{\Phi}\gg m_{1}
.

19

Dual theory. Unbroken flavor symmetry

19.1

L - vacua,
\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}/N_{F}\ll 1

Let us recall, see e.g. section 4 in
[
20
]
,
that the Lagrangian of the dual theory at
\mu=\Lambda_{Q}
and

0<\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}/N_{F}\ll 1,\,\,\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}=3{\overline{N}}_{c}-N_{F}
, looks as

K={\rm Tr}\Bigl(q^{\dagger}q+(q\rightarrow\overline{q})\Bigr)+{\rm Tr}\,\frac{M^{\dagger}M}{Z_{q}^{2}\Lambda_{Q}^{2}}\,,\,\,{\cal W}=\,-\,\frac{2\pi}{\overline{\alpha}(\mu=\Lambda_{Q})}\,{\overline{s}}+{\cal W}_{M}+{\cal W}_{q},\,Z_{q}\sim\exp\Bigl\{-\frac{{\overline{N}}_{c}}{7\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}}\Bigr\}\ll 1\,,

\displaystyle{\cal W}_{M}=m_{Q}{\rm Tr}\,M-\frac{1}{2\mu_{\Phi}}\Biggl[{\rm Tr}\,(M^{2})-\frac{1}{N_{c}}({\rm Tr}\,M)^{2}\Biggr]\,,\hskip 11.74988pt{\cal W}_{q}=-\,\frac{1}{Z_{q}\Lambda_{Q}}\,\rm{Tr}\Bigl({\overline{q}}\,M\,q\Bigr)\,.

(19.1.1)

Because
\Lambda_{Q}^{2}/\mu_{\Phi}\ll\Lambda_{Q}
, the mions are effectively massless
and dynamically relevant at
\mu\sim\Lambda_{Q}
(and so in some range
of scales below
\Lambda_{Q}
). By definition,
\mu\sim\Lambda_{Q}
is such a
scale that the dual theory already entered sufficiently deep the
conformal regime, i.e. the dual gauge coupling
{\overline{a}}(\mu=\Lambda_{Q})={\overline{N}}_{c}{\overline{\alpha}}(\mu=\Lambda_{Q})/2\pi
is sufficiently close
to its small frozen value,
\overline{\delta}=[{\overline{a}}_{*}-{\overline{a}}(\mu\sim\Lambda_{Q})]/{\overline{a}}_{*}\ll 1
, and
\overline{\delta}
is neglected
everywhere below in comparison with 1 for simplicity (and the
same for the Yukawa coupling
{\overline{a}}_{f}={\overline{N}}_{c}{\overline{\alpha}}_{f}/2\pi
),
see
[
20
]
and Appendix therein). The fixed point value
of the dual gauge coupling is
{\overline{a}}_{*}\approx 7\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}/3{\overline{N}}_{c}\ll 1

[
16
]
.

We recall also that the mion condensates are matched to the
condensates of direct quarks
in all vacua
,
\langle M^{i}_{j}(\mu=\Lambda_{Q})\rangle=\langle{\overline{Q}}_{j}Q^{i}(\mu=\Lambda_{Q})\rangle
.
Hence, in these L - vacua

\displaystyle\langle M\rangle_{L}\sim\Lambda_{Q}^{2}\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\Bigr)^{\frac{{\overline{N}}_{c}}{2N_{c}-N_{F}}}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\langle N\rangle_{L}\equiv\langle{\overline{q}}q(\mu=\Lambda_{Q})\rangle=\frac{Z_{q}\Lambda_{Q}\langle S\rangle_{L}}{\langle M\rangle_{L}}\sim Z_{q}\Lambda_{Q}^{2}\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\Bigr)^{\frac{N_{c}}{2N_{c}-N_{F}}}\,,

(19.1.2)

Z_{q}\sim\exp\Bigl\{-\frac{1}{3{\overline{a}}_{*}}\Bigr\}\sim\exp\Bigl\{-\frac{{\overline{N}}_{c}}{7\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}}\Bigr\}\ll 1,

and here and everywhere below, as in
[
20
]
, a parametric
dependence on the small parameter
\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}/N_{F}\ll 1
is traced with
an exponential accuracy only (i.e. powers of
\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}/N_{F}
are not
traced, only powers of
Z_{q}
).

The current mass
\mu_{q,L}\equiv\mu_{q,L}(\mu=\Lambda_{Q})
of dual
quarks
{\overline{q}},\,q
and their pole mass in these
(2N_{c}-N_{F})
L
- vacua are, see (
19.1.1
),

\frac{\mu_{q,L}}{\Lambda_{Q}}=\frac{\langle M\rangle_{L}}{Z_{q}\Lambda_{Q}^{2}}\sim\frac{1}{Z_{q}}\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\Bigr)^{\frac{{\overline{N}}_{c}}{2N_{c}-N_{F}}}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,L}=\frac{\mu_{q,L}}{z_{q}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,L})}\,,\hskip 11.74988ptz_{q}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,L})\sim\Bigl(\frac{\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,L}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}/N_{F}}\,,

\displaystyle\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,L}\sim\Lambda_{Q}\Bigl(\frac{\mu_{q,L}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{N_{F}/3{\overline{N}}_{c}}\sim\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{Z_{q}}\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\Bigr)^{\frac{N_{F}}{3(2N_{c}-N_{F})}}\sim\frac{1}{Z_{q}}{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(L)}\gg{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(L)}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\Lambda_{Q}\ll\mu_{\Phi}\ll m_{1}\,,

(19.1.3)

\frac{\mu_{q,L}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\sim\frac{1}{Z_{q}}\Bigl(\frac{m_{Q}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\frac{{\overline{N}}_{c}}{N_{c}}}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,L}\sim\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{Z_{q}}\Bigl(\frac{m_{Q}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\frac{N_{F}}{3N_{c}}}\sim\frac{1}{Z_{q}}{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(SQCD)}\gg{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(SQCD)}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\mu_{\Phi}\gg m_{1}\,,

while the gluon mass due to possible higgsing of dual quarks
looks at
\Lambda_{Q}\ll\mu_{\Phi}\ll m_{1}
as

\displaystyle{\overline{\mu}}_{\rm gl,L}\sim\Bigl[{\overline{a}}_{*}\langle N\rangle_{L}\,z_{q}(\Lambda_{Q},{\overline{\mu}}_{\rm gl}\Bigr]^{1/2}\sim Z_{q}^{1/2}\Lambda_{Q}\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\Bigr)^{N_{F}/3(2N_{c}-N_{F})}\sim Z_{q}^{3/2}\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,L}\ll\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,L}\,.

(19.1.4)

Therefore, the dual quarks are definitely in the HQ phase in
these L - vacua at
\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}/{\overline{N}}_{c}\ll 1
.

With decreasing scale the perturbative running mass

\mu_{M}(\mu)
of mions

\displaystyle\mu_{M}(\mu)\sim\frac{Z_{q}^{2}\Lambda_{Q}^{2}}{\mu_{\Phi}z_{M}(\mu)}=\frac{Z_{q}^{2}\Lambda_{Q}^{2}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\Bigl(\frac{\mu}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{2\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}/N_{F}}

(19.1.5)

decreases but more slowly than the scale
\mu
itself because

\gamma_{M}=-(2{\rm\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}}/N_{F}),\,\,|\gamma_{M}|<1
at

3/2<N_{F}/N_{c}<2
, and
\mu_{M}(\mu)
becomes frozen at

\mu<\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,L}
,
\mu_{M}(\mu<\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,L})=\mu_{M}(\mu=\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,L})
.

After integrating out all dual quarks as heavy ones at

\mu<\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,L}
and then all
SU({\overline{N}}_{c})
gluons at

\mu<{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(L)}
via the Veneziano-Yankielowicz (VY) procedure

[
15
]
, the Lagrangian of mions looks as

K=\frac{z^{(L)}_{M}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,L})}{Z_{q}^{2}\Lambda_{Q}^{2}}{\rm Tr}\,(M^{\dagger}M)\,,\hskip 11.74988ptz^{(L)}_{M}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,L})\sim\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,L}}\Bigr)^{2\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}/N_{F}}\,,\hskip 11.74988ptS=\Biggl(\,\frac{\det M}{\Lambda_{Q}^{\rm{b_{o}}}}\,\Biggr)^{1/{\overline{N}}_{c}},

\displaystyle W=-{\overline{N}}_{c}S+m_{Q}{\rm Tr}\,M-\frac{1}{2\mu_{\Phi}}\Biggl[{\rm Tr}\,(M^{2})-\frac{1}{N_{c}}({\rm Tr}\,M)^{2}\Biggr]\,.

(19.1.6)

There are two contributions to the mass of mions in
(
19.1.6
), the perturbative one from the term
\sim M^{2}/\mu_{\Phi}
and non-perturbative one from
\sim S
.
Both are parametrically the same and the total contribution
looks as

\displaystyle\mu^{\rm pole}(M)\sim\frac{Z_{q}^{2}\Lambda_{Q}^{2}}{z_{M}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,L})\mu_{\Phi}}\sim Z_{q}^{2}{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(L)}\ll{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(L)}\ll\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,L}\,,

(19.1.7)

and this parametrical hierarchy guarantees that the mass

\mu^{\rm pole}(M)
in (
19.1.7
) is indeed the
pole
mass
of mions.

On the whole, the mass spectrum in these dual L - vacua looks as
follows at
\Lambda_{Q}\ll\mu_{\Phi}\ll m_{1}
. a) There is a large number of
heaviest flavored hadrons made of weakly interacting and weakly
confined (the tension of the confining string originating from
the unbroken
SU({\overline{N}}_{c})
SYM is

\sqrt{\sigma}\sim{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(L)}\ll\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,L}
)
non-relativistic quarks
{\overline{q}},q
with the pole masses

\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,L}/{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(L)}\sim\exp({\overline{N}}_{c}/7\rm{\overline{b}}_{o})\gg 1
. The
mass spectrum of low-lying flavored mesons is Coulomb-like with
parametrically small mass differences

\Delta\mu_{H}/\mu_{H}=O(\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}^{2}/{\overline{N}}_{c}^{2})\ll 1
. b) A large number of
gluonia made of
SU({\overline{N}}_{c})
gluons with the mass scale

\sim{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(L)}\sim\Lambda_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q}/\mu_{\Phi})^{N_{F}/3(2N_{c}-N_{F})}
. c)
N_{F}^{2}

lightest mions with parametrically smaller masses
\mu^{\rm pole}(M)/{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(L)}\sim\exp(-2{\overline{N}}_{c}/7\rm{\overline{b}}_{o})\ll 1
.

At
\mu_{\Phi}\gg m_{1}
these L - vacua evolve into the vacua of the
dual SQCD theory (dSQCD), see section 4 in
[
20
]
.

19.2

S - vacua,
\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}/N_{F}\ll 1

The current mass
\mu_{q,S}\equiv\mu_{q,S}(\mu=\Lambda_{Q})
of dual
quarks
{\overline{q}},\,q
at the scale
\mu=\Lambda_{Q}
in these
(N_{F}-N_{c})

dual S-vacua is, see (
16.1.2
),

\displaystyle\mu_{q,S}=\frac{\langle M\rangle_{S}=\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)\rangle_{S}}{Z_{q}\Lambda_{Q}}\sim\frac{m_{Q}\mu_{\Phi}}{Z_{q}\Lambda_{Q}}\,,\hskip 11.74988ptZ_{q}\sim\exp\Bigl\{-\frac{{\overline{N}}_{c}}{7\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}}\Bigr\}\ll 1\,.

(19.2.1)

In comparison with the L - vacua in section 19.1, a qualitatively
new element here is that
\mu^{\rm pole}(M)
is the largest
mass,
\mu^{\rm pole}(M)\gg\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,S}
, in the wide
region
\Lambda_{Q}\ll\mu_{\Phi}\ll Z_{q}^{\,3/2}m_{1}
. In this region: a) the mions
are effectively massless
and dynamically relevant at scales
\mu^{\rm pole}(M)\ll\mu\ll\Lambda_{Q}
,   b) there is a pole in the mion
propagator at the momentum
p=\mu^{\rm pole}(M)
,

\mu^{\rm pole}(M)=\frac{Z_{q}^{2}\Lambda_{Q}^{2}}{z_{M}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu^{\rm pole}(M))\mu_{\Phi}}\,,\hskip 11.74988ptz_{M}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu^{\rm pole}(M))\sim\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{\mu^{\rm pole}(M)}\Bigr)^{2\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}/N_{F}}\,,

\displaystyle\mu^{\rm pole}(M)\sim Z_{q}^{2}\Lambda_{Q}\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\Bigr)^{\frac{N_{F}}{3(2N_{c}-N_{F})}}\,,\hskip 11.74988ptZ_{q}^{2}\sim\exp\{-\frac{2{\overline{N}}_{c}}{7\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}}\}\ll 1\,.

(19.2.2)

The mions then become too heavy and dynamically irrelevant at

\mu\ll\mu^{\rm pole}(M)
. Due to this, they decouple from the
RG evolution of dual quarks and gluons and from the ’t Hooft
triangles, and (at
\mu_{\Phi}
not too close to
m_{1}
to have enough
”time” to evolve, see (
19.2.7
) ) the remained dual theory
of
N_{F}
quarks
{\overline{q}},q
and
SU({\overline{N}}_{c})
gluons evolves into a
new conformal regime with a new smaller value of the frozen
gauge coupling,
{\overline{a}}^{\,\prime}_{*}\approx\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}/3{\overline{N}}_{c}
.
It is worth noting that, in spite of that mions
are dynamically irrelevant at
\mu<\mu^{\rm pole}(M)
, their
renormalization factor
z_{M}(\mu<\mu^{\rm pole}(M))
still runs
in the range of scales
\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,S}<\mu<\mu^{\rm pole}(M)
being induced by loops of still effectively massless
dual quarks and gluons.

The next physical scale is the perturbative pole mass of dual
quarks

\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,S}=\frac{\langle M\rangle_{S}}{Z_{q}\Lambda_{Q}}\,\frac{1}{z_{q}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,S})}\,,\hskip 11.74988ptz_{q}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,S})=\Bigl(\frac{\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,S}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}/N_{F}}{\overline{\rho}}_{S}\,,

{\overline{\rho}}_{S}=\Bigl(\frac{{\overline{a}}_{*}}{{\overline{a}}^{\,\prime}_{*}}\Bigr)^{\frac{{\overline{N}}_{c}}{N_{F}}}\exp\Bigl\{\frac{{\overline{N}}_{c}}{N_{F}}\Bigl(\frac{1}{{\overline{a}}_{*}}-\frac{1}{{\overline{a}}^{\,\prime}_{*}}\Bigr)\Bigr\}\sim\frac{{\overline{Z}}_{q}}{Z_{q}}\ll 1\,,\hskip 11.74988pt{\overline{Z}}_{q}\sim\exp\Bigl\{-\frac{{\overline{N}}_{c}}{\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}}\Bigr\}\sim\Bigl(Z_{q}\Bigr)^{7}\ll Z_{q}\,,

\displaystyle\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,S}\sim\frac{1}{{\overline{Z}}_{q}}{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(S)}\gg{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(S)}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(S)}=\Lambda_{Q}\Bigl(\frac{m_{Q}\mu_{\Phi}}{\Lambda_{Q}^{2}}\Bigr)^{N_{F}/3{\overline{N}}_{c}}\,.

(19.2.3)

This has to be compared with the gluon mass due to possible
higgsing of
{\overline{q}},q

\displaystyle{\overline{\mu}}^{\,2}_{\rm gl,S}\sim z_{q}(\Lambda_{Q},{\overline{\mu}}_{\rm gl,S})\langle{\overline{q}}q\rangle_{S}\,\,\rightarrow\,\,{\overline{\mu}}_{\rm gl,S}\sim{\overline{Z}}_{q}^{\,1/2}{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(S)}\ll{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(S)}\ll\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,S}.

(19.2.4)

The parametric hierarchy in (
19.2.4
) guarantees that the
dual quarks are in the HQ phase in these S - vacua.

Hence, after integrating out all quarks at
\mu<\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,S}
and, finally,
SU({\overline{N}}_{c})
gluons at
\mu<{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(S)}
,
the Lagrangian looks as in (
19.1.6
) but with a replacement

z^{(L)}_{M}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,L})\rightarrow z^{(S)}_{M}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,S})
,

\displaystyle z^{(S)}_{M}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,S})=\frac{1}{z^{2}_{q}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,S})}\sim\frac{1}{z^{2}_{q}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,S})}\sim\frac{Z_{q}^{\,2}}{{\overline{Z}}_{q}^{\,2}}\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,S}}\Bigr)^{2\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}/N_{F}}\,.

(19.2.5)

The contribution of the term
\sim M^{2}/\mu_{\Phi}
in the
superpotential (
19.1.6
) to the frozen low energy value

\mu(M)
of the running mion mass is dominant at
\mu_{\Phi}/m_{1}\ll 1

and is

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636pt\mu(M)=\frac{Z_{q}^{2}\Lambda_{Q}^{2}}{z^{(S)}_{M}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,S})\mu_{\Phi}}\sim\frac{{\overline{Z}}_{q}^{\,2}\Lambda_{Q}^{2}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\Bigl(\frac{\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,S}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\frac{2\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}}{N_{F}}}\ll\mu^{\rm pole}(M)\,.

(19.2.6)

The requirement of self-consistency looks in this case as

\displaystyle\frac{\mu(M)}{\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,S}}\sim{\overline{Z}}_{q}^{\,3}\Bigl(\frac{m_{1}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\Bigr)^{N_{c}/{\overline{N}}_{c}}\gg 1\hskip 11.74988pt\rightarrow\hskip 11.74988pt\frac{\mu_{\Phi}}{m_{1}}\ll{\overline{Z}}^{\,3/2}_{q}\sim\exp\Bigl\{-\frac{3{\overline{N}}_{c}}{2\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}}\Bigr\}\ll Z_{q}^{3/2}\,,

(19.2.7)

the meaning of (
19.2.7
) is that only at this condition the
range of scales between
\mu^{\rm pole}(M)
in (
19.2.2
) and

\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,S}\ll\mu^{\rm pole}(M)
in (
19.2.3
) is
sufficiently large that theory has enough ”time” to evolve from

{\overline{a}}_{*}=7\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}/3{\overline{N}}_{c}
to
{\overline{a}}^{\,\prime}_{*}=\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}/3{\overline{N}}_{c}
. There
is no pole in the mion propagator at the momentum

p=\mu(M)\gg\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,S}
.

The opposite case with
\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,S}\gg\mu^{\rm pole}(M)
is realized if the ratio
\mu_{\Phi}/m_{1}
is still
\ll 1

but is much larger than
Z_{q}^{\,3/2}\gg{\overline{Z}}_{q}^{\,3/2}
, see
(
19.2.8
) below. In this case the theory at
\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,S}<\mu<\Lambda_{Q}
remains in the conformal regime with
{\overline{a}}_{*}=7\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}/3{\overline{N}}_{c}
and the largest mass is
\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,S}
.
One has in this case instead of
(
19.2.2
),(
19.2.3
),(
19.2.7
)

{\overline{\rho}}_{S}\sim 1\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,S}\sim\frac{1}{Z_{q}}{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(S)}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\frac{\mu^{\rm pole}(M)}{\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,S}}\sim Z^{3}_{q}\Bigl(\frac{m_{1}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\Bigr)^{N_{c}/{\overline{N}}_{c}}\,,

\displaystyle\frac{\mu^{\rm pole}(M)}{\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,S}}\ll 1\hskip 11.74988pt\rightarrow\hskip 11.74988ptZ_{q}^{\,3/2}\ll\frac{\mu_{\Phi}}{m_{1}}\ll 1\,.

(19.2.8)

On the whole, the mass spectrum in these
{\overline{N}}_{c}
dual S - vacua
looks as follows at
\Lambda_{Q}\ll\mu_{\Phi}\ll{\overline{Z}}_{q}^{\,3/2}m_{1}
. a) The
heaviest are
N_{F}^{2}
mions with the pole masses (
19.2.2
).
b) There is a large number of flavored hadrons made of weakly
interacting and weakly confined (the tension of the confining
string is
\sqrt{\sigma}\sim{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(S)}\ll\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,S}\ll\mu^{\rm pole}(M)
) non-relativistic dual quarks

{\overline{q}},q
with the perturbative pole masses (
19.2.3
).
The mass spectrum of low-lying flavored mesons is Coulomb-like
with parametrically small mass differences

\Delta\mu_{H}/\mu_{H}=O(\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}^{2}/{\overline{N}}_{c}^{2})\ll 1
. b) A large number of
gluonia made of
SU({\overline{N}}_{c})
gluons with the mass scale

\sim{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(S)}\sim\Lambda_{Q}(m_{Q}\mu_{\Phi}/\Lambda_{Q}^{2})^{N_{F}/3{\overline{N}}_{c}}
.

The mions with the pole masses (
19.2.2
) remain the heaviest
ones,
\mu^{\rm pole}(M)\gg\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,S}
, at values

\mu_{\Phi}
in the range
{\overline{Z}}_{q}^{\,3/2}m_{1}\ll\mu_{\Phi}\ll Z_{q}^{\,3/2}m_{1}
, while the value
\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,S}
varies
in a range
{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(S)}/Z_{q}\ll\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,S}\ll{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(S)}/{\overline{Z}}_{q}
. Finally, in a close
vicinity of
m_{1},\,\,Z_{q}^{\,3/2}m_{1}\ll\mu_{\Phi}\ll m_{1}
, the
perturbative pole mass of quarks,
\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,S}\sim{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(S)}/Z_{q}\gg{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(S)}
, becomes the largest one, while the pole
masses of mions
\mu^{\rm pole}(M)\ll\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,S}

become as in (
19.2.8
).

At
\mu_{\Phi}\gg m_{1}
these S - vacua evolve into the vacua of dSQCD,
see section 4 in
[
20
]
.

20

Direct theory. Broken flavor symmetry,

\mathbf{\Lambda_{Q}\ll\mu_{\Phi}\ll m_{1}}

20.1

L - type vacua

The quark condensates are parametrically the same
as in the L - vacua with unbroken flavor symmetry in section 18,

\displaystyle(1-\frac{n_{1}}{N_{c}})\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{Lt}\approx-(1-\frac{n_{2}}{N_{c}})\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{Lt},\hskip 11.74988pt\langle S\rangle=\frac{\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle}{\mu_{\Phi}}\,,

(20.1.1)

\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{Lt}\sim\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{Lt}\sim\Lambda_{Q}^{2}\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\Bigr)^{\frac{{\overline{N}}_{c}}{2N_{c}-N_{F}}}\,.
