All quarks are in the HQ phase and are confined and the
Lagrangian of fions looks as in (
18.1.3
), but one has to
choose the L - type vacua with the broken flavor symmetry in
(
18.1.3
). Due to this, the masses of
hybrid fions
\Phi_{12},\Phi_{21}
are qualitatively different,
they are the Nambu-Goldstone particles here and are massless.
The ”masses” of
\Phi_{11}
and
\Phi_{22}
are parametrically
as in (
18.1.4
),

\displaystyle\mu(\Phi_{11})\sim\mu(\Phi_{22})\sim\frac{\mu_{\Phi}}{z_{\Phi}(\Lambda_{Q},m^{\rm pole}_{Q})}\sim m^{\rm pole}_{Q,1}\sim m^{\rm pole}_{Q,2}\sim{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(L)}\sim\Lambda_{Q}\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\Bigr)^{\frac{N_{F}}{3(2N_{c}-N_{F})}}\,,

(20.1.2)

and hence there is no guaranty that these are the pole masses of
fions, see section 18. May be yes, but maybe not.

On the whole, there are only two characteristic scales in the
mass spectra in these L - type vacua. The hybrid fions

\Phi_{12},\Phi_{21}
are massless
while all other masses are
\sim{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(L)}
.

20.2

\rm br2
vacua

The condensates of quarks look as

\displaystyle\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{\rm br2}\approx\Bigl(\rho_{2}=-\frac{n_{2}-N_{c}}{N_{c}}\Bigr)m_{Q}\mu_{\Phi},\,\,\,\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{\rm br2}\sim\Lambda_{Q}^{2}\Bigl(\frac{\mu_{\Phi}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\frac{n_{2}}{n_{2}-N_{c}}}\Bigl(\frac{m_{Q}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\frac{N_{c}-n_{1}}{n_{2}-N_{c}}},

(20.2.1)

\frac{\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{\rm br2}}{\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{\rm br2}}\sim\Bigl(\frac{\mu_{\Phi}}{m_{1}}\Bigr)^{\frac{N_{c}}{n_{2}-N_{c}}}\ll 1

in these vacua with
n_{2}>N_{c}\,,1\leq n_{1}<{\overline{N}}_{c}
. Hence, the
largest among the masses smaller than
\Lambda_{Q}
are the masses of
the
N_{F}^{2}
second generation fions, see (
17.0.1
),

\displaystyle\mu^{\rm pole}_{2}(\Phi_{i}^{j})=\mu_{o}^{\rm conf}\sim\Lambda_{Q}\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\Bigr)^{\frac{N_{F}}{3(2N_{c}-N_{F})}}\,,

(20.2.2)

while some other possible characteristic masses look here as

\displaystyle\langle m^{\rm tot}_{Q,1}\rangle_{\rm br2}=\frac{\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{\rm br2}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\sim m_{Q}\,,\hskip 11.74988ptm^{\rm pole}_{Q,1}\sim\Lambda_{Q}\Bigl(\frac{m_{Q}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{N_{F}/3N_{c}}\gg{\tilde{m}}^{\rm pole}_{Q,2}\,,

(20.2.3)

\mu^{2}_{\rm gl,2}\sim z_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu_{\rm gl,2})\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{\rm br2},\hskip 11.74988ptz_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu_{\rm gl,2})\sim\Bigl(\frac{\mu_{\rm gl,2}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\frac{\rm{b_{o}}}{N_{F}}}\ll 1\,,

\displaystyle\mu_{\rm gl,2}\sim\Lambda_{Q}\Bigl(\frac{m_{Q}\mu_{\Phi}}{\Lambda_{Q}^{2}}\Bigr)^{N_{F}/3{\overline{N}}_{c}}\gg\mu_{\rm gl,1}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\frac{\mu_{\rm gl,2}}{m^{\rm pole}_{Q,1}}\sim\Bigl(\frac{\mu_{\Phi}}{m_{1}}\Bigr)^{\frac{N_{F}}{3{\overline{N}}_{c}}}\ll 1\,,

(20.2.4)

where
m^{\rm pole}_{Q,1}
and
{\tilde{m}}^{\rm pole}_{Q,2}
are
the pole masses of quarks
{\overline{Q}}_{1},Q^{1}
and
{\overline{Q}}_{2},Q^{2}

and
\mu_{\rm gl,1},\,\mu_{\rm gl,2}
are the gluon masses due to
possible higgsing of these quarks. Hence, the largest mass is

m^{\rm pole}_{Q,1}
and the overall phase is
HQ_{1}-HQ_{2}
.

The lower energy theory at
\mu<m^{\rm pole}_{Q,1}
has
N_{c}

colors and
N_{F}^{\prime}=n_{2}>N_{c}
flavors of quarks
{\overline{Q}}_{2},Q^{2}
. In the range of scales
m^{\rm pole}_{Q,2}<\mu<m^{\rm pole}_{Q,1}
, it will remain in the conformal regime at

n_{1}<\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}=(2N_{F}-3N_{c})/2
, while it will be in the strong coupling
regime at
n_{1}>\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}/2
, with the gauge coupling
a(\mu\ll m^{\rm pole}_{Q,1})\gg 1
. We do not consider the strong coupling
regime here and for this reason we take
\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}/{\overline{N}}_{c}=O(1)

in this subsection and consider
n_{1}<\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}/2
only.

After the heaviest quarks
{\overline{Q}}_{1},Q^{1}
decouple at

\mu<m^{\rm pole}_{Q,1}
, the pole mass of quarks
{\overline{Q}}_{2},Q^{2}
in the lower energy theory looks as

\displaystyle m^{\rm pole}_{Q,2}=\frac{1}{z^{\,\prime}_{Q}(m^{\rm pole}_{Q,1},m^{\rm pole}_{Q,2})}\Biggl(\,\frac{\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{\rm br2}}{\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{\rm br2}}\,m^{\rm pole}_{Q,1}\,\Biggr)\sim{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(\rm br2)}\,,

(20.2.5)

z^{\,\prime}_{Q}(m^{\rm pole}_{Q,1},m^{\rm pole}_{Q,2})\sim\Bigl(\frac{m^{\rm pole}_{Q,2}}{m^{\rm pole}_{Q,1}}\Bigr)^{\frac{3N_{c}-n_{2}}{n_{2}}}\ll 1\,.

Hence, after integrating out quarks
{\overline{Q}}_{1},Q^{1}
at

\mu<m^{\rm pole}_{Q,1}
and then quarks
{\overline{Q}}_{2},Q^{2}
and

SU(N_{c})
gluons at
\mu<{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(\rm br2)}
, the Lagrangian of
fions looks as

\displaystyle K=z_{\Phi}(\Lambda_{Q},m^{\rm pole}_{Q,1})\,{\rm Tr}\,\Bigl[\,\Phi_{11}^{\dagger}\Phi_{11}+\Phi_{12}^{\dagger}\Phi_{12}+\Phi_{21}^{\dagger}\Phi_{21}+z^{\,\prime}_{\Phi}(m^{\rm pole}_{Q,1},m^{\rm pole}_{Q,2})\Phi_{22}^{\dagger}\Phi_{22}\,\Bigr]\,,

(20.2.6)

z_{\Phi}(\Lambda_{Q},m^{\rm pole}_{Q,1})\sim\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{m^{\rm pole}_{Q,1}}\Bigr)^{\frac{2(3N_{c}-N_{F})}{N_{F}}}\gg 1\,,\hskip 11.74988ptz^{\,\prime}_{\Phi}(m^{\rm pole}_{Q,1},m^{\rm pole}_{Q,2})\sim\Bigl(\frac{m^{\rm pole}_{Q,1}}{m^{\rm pole}_{Q,2}}\Bigr)^{\frac{2(3N_{c}-n_{2})}{n_{2}}}\gg 1\,,

\displaystyle{\cal W}=N_{c}S+{\cal W}_{\Phi}\,,\hskip 11.74988ptm^{\rm tot}_{Q}=(m_{Q}-\Phi)\,,\hskip 11.74988pt

(20.2.7)

S=\Bigl(\Lambda_{Q}^{\rm{b_{o}}}\det m^{\rm tot}_{Q}\Bigr)^{1/N_{c}}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt{\cal W}_{\Phi}=\frac{\mu_{\Phi}}{2}\Bigl({\rm Tr}\,(\Phi^{2})-\frac{1}{{\overline{N}}_{c}}({\rm Tr}\,\Phi)^{2}\,\Bigr).

From (
20.2.6
),(
20.2.7
), the main contribution to the
mass of the third generation fions
\Phi_{11}
gives the term

\sim\mu_{\Phi}\Phi^{2}_{11}
,

\displaystyle\mu^{\rm pole}_{3}(\Phi_{11})\sim\frac{\mu_{\Phi}}{z_{\Phi}(\Lambda_{Q},m^{\rm pole}_{Q,1})}\sim\Bigl(\frac{\mu_{\Phi}}{m_{1}}\Bigr)m^{\rm pole}_{Q,1}\,,

(20.2.8)

while the third generation hybrid fions
\Phi_{12},\Phi_{21}

are massless,
\mu^{\rm pole}_{3}(\Phi_{12})=\mu^{\rm pole}_{3}(\Phi_{21})=0
. As for the third generation fions

\Phi_{22}
, the main contribution to their masses comes from
the non-perturbative term
\sim S
in the superpotential
(
20.2.7
)

\displaystyle\mu_{3}(\Phi_{22})\sim\frac{\langle S\rangle}{\langle m^{\rm tot}_{Q,2}\rangle^{2}}\frac{1}{z_{\Phi}(\Lambda_{Q},m^{\rm pole}_{Q,1})z^{\,\prime}_{\Phi}(m^{\rm pole}_{Q,1},m^{\rm pole}_{Q,2})}\sim m^{\rm pole}_{Q,2}\sim{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(\rm br2)}.

(20.2.9)

In such a situation there is no guaranty that there is a pole in
the propagator of
\Phi_{22}
at the momentum
p\sim m^{\rm pole}_{Q,2}
. May be yes but maybe not, see section 17.

21

Dual theory. Broken flavor symmetry,

\mathbf{\Lambda_{Q}\ll\mu_{\Phi}\ll m_{1}}

21.1

L - type vacua,
\,\,\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}/N_{F}\ll 1

The condensates of mions and dual quarks look here
as

\langle M_{1}+M_{2}-\frac{1}{N_{c}}{\rm Tr}\,M\rangle_{Lt}=m_{Q}\mu_{\Phi}\hskip 11.74988pt\rightarrow\hskip 11.74988pt\frac{\langle M_{1}\rangle_{Lt}}{\langle M_{2}\rangle_{Lt}}\approx-\,\frac{N_{c}-n_{1}}{N_{c}-n_{2}}\,,

\langle M_{1}\rangle_{Lt}\langle({\overline{q}}q)_{1}\rangle_{Lt}=\langle M_{2}\rangle_{Lt}\langle{(\overline{q}}q)_{2}\rangle_{Lt}=Z_{q}\Lambda_{Q}\langle S\rangle_{Lt},\hskip 11.74988pt\langle S\rangle_{Lt}=\frac{\langle M_{1}\rangle_{Lt}\langle M_{2}\rangle_{Lt}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\,.

I.e., all condensates are parametrically the same as in the L -
vacua with unbroken flavor symmetry in section 19.1 and the
overall phase is also
HQ_{1}-HQ_{2}
. The pole masses of dual
quarks are as in (
19.1.3
), the Lagrangian of mions is as in
(
19.1.6
) and the pole masses of mions
M_{11}
and
M_{22}

are as in (
19.1.7
). But the masses of hybrid mions
M_{12}

and
M_{21}
are qualitatively different here. They are the
Nambu-Goldstone particles now and are exactly massless,

\mu(M_{12})=\mu(M_{21})=0
.

21.2

\rm br2
vacua,
\,\,\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}/N_{F}=O(1)

In these vacua with
n_{2}>N_{c}\,,1\leq n_{1}<{\overline{N}}_{c}
the
condensates of mions and dual quarks look as

\langle M_{1}\rangle_{\rm br2}=\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{\rm br2}\sim\Lambda_{Q}^{2}\Bigl(\frac{\mu_{\Phi}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\frac{n_{2}}{n_{2}-N_{c}}}\Bigl(\frac{m_{Q}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\frac{N_{c}-n_{1}}{n_{2}-N_{c}}},

\displaystyle\langle M_{2}\rangle_{\rm br2}=\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{\rm br2}\approx-\,\frac{n_{2}-N_{c}}{N_{c}}\,m_{Q}\mu_{\Phi}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\frac{\langle M_{1}\rangle_{\rm br2}}{\langle M_{2}\rangle_{\rm br2}}\sim\Bigl(\frac{\mu_{\Phi}}{m_{1}}\Bigr)^{\frac{N_{c}}{n_{2}-N_{c}}}\ll 1\,,

(21.2.1)

\langle({\overline{q}}q)_{1}\rangle_{\rm br2}=\langle{\overline{q}}^{1}q_{1}(\mu=\Lambda_{Q})\rangle_{\rm br2}=\frac{\Lambda_{Q}\langle S\rangle_{\rm br2}}{\langle M_{1}\rangle_{\rm br2}}=\frac{\Lambda_{Q}\langle M_{2}\rangle_{\rm br2}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\sim m_{Q}\Lambda_{Q}\gg\langle{(\overline{q}}q)_{2}\rangle_{\rm br2}\,.

From these, the heaviest are
N_{F}^{2}
mions
M^{i}_{j}
with the pole
masses

\displaystyle\hskip-11.38109pt\mu^{\rm pole}(M)=\frac{\Lambda_{Q}^{2}/\mu_{\Phi}}{z_{M}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu^{\rm pole}(M))}\sim\Lambda_{Q}\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\Bigr)^{\frac{N_{F}}{3(2N_{c}-N_{F})}}\,,

(21.2.2)

z_{M}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu^{\rm pole}(M))\sim\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{\mu^{\rm pole}(M)}\Bigr)^{\frac{2\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}}{N_{F}}}\gg 1,\hskip 11.74988pt\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}=3{\overline{N}}_{c}-N_{F}\,,

while some other possible characteristic masses look as

\displaystyle\mu_{q,2}=\frac{\langle M_{2}\rangle}{\Lambda_{Q}}\sim\frac{m_{Q}\mu_{\Phi}}{\Lambda_{Q}},\hskip 11.74988pt{\tilde{\mu}}^{\rm pole}_{q,2}\sim\Lambda_{Q}\Bigl(\frac{m_{Q}\mu_{\Phi}}{\Lambda_{Q}^{2}}\Bigr)^{N_{F}/3{\overline{N}}_{c}}\gg\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,1}\,,

(21.2.3)

{\overline{\mu}}_{\rm gl,1}\sim\Lambda_{Q}\Bigl(\frac{\langle({\overline{q}}q)_{1}\rangle}{\Lambda_{Q}^{2}}\Bigr)^{N_{F}/3N_{c}}\sim\Lambda_{Q}\Bigl(\frac{m_{Q}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{N_{F}/3N_{c}}\gg{\overline{\mu}}_{\rm gl,2}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\frac{{\overline{\mu}}_{\rm gl,1}}{{\tilde{\mu}}^{\rm pole}_{q,2}}\sim\Bigl(\frac{m_{1}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\Bigr)^{N_{F}/3{\overline{N}}_{c}}\gg 1\,,

where
\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,1}
and
{\tilde{\mu}}^{\rm pole}_{q,2}

are the perturbative pole masses of quarks
{\overline{q}}^{1},q_{1}
and

{\overline{q}}^{2},q_{2}
and
{\overline{\mu}}_{\rm gl,1},\,{\overline{\mu}}_{\rm gl,2}
are the gluon masses due to possible higgsing of these
quarks. Hence, the largest mass is
{\overline{\mu}}_{\rm gl,1}
and the
overall phase is
Higgs_{1}-HQ_{2}
.

After integrating out all higgsed quarks
{\overline{q}}^{1},q_{1}
and heavy
gluons, we write the dual Lagrangian at

\mu={\overline{\mu}}_{{\rm gl},\,1}
as

K=z_{M}(\Lambda_{Q},{\overline{\mu}}_{{\rm gl},\,1}){\rm Tr}\,\frac{M^{\dagger}M}{\Lambda_{Q}^{2}}+z_{q}(\Lambda_{Q},{\overline{\mu}}_{{\rm gl},\,1}){\rm Tr}\,\Bigl[\,2\sqrt{N_{11}^{\dagger}N_{11}}+K_{\rm hybr}+\Bigl({\textsf{q}}^{\dagger}_{2}{\textsf{q}}_{2}+({\textsf{q}}_{2}\rightarrow{\overline{\textsf{q}}}^{2})\Bigr)\,\Bigr]\,,

\displaystyle K_{\rm hybr}=\Biggl(N^{\dagger}_{12}\frac{1}{\sqrt{N_{11}N^{\dagger}_{11}}}N_{12}+N_{21}\frac{1}{\sqrt{N^{\dagger}_{11}N_{11}}}N^{\dagger}_{21}\Biggr),\hskip 11.74988ptz_{q}(\Lambda_{Q},{\overline{\mu}}_{{\rm gl},\,1})=\Bigl(\frac{{\overline{\mu}}_{{\rm gl},\,1}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}/N_{F}}\,,

(21.2.4)

z_{M}(\Lambda_{Q},{\overline{\mu}}_{{\rm gl},\,1})=1/z^{2}_{q}(\Lambda_{Q},{\overline{\mu}}_{{\rm gl},\,1}),\hskip 11.74988pt{\cal W}=\Bigl[\frac{2\pi}{{\overline{\alpha}}(\mu)}{\overline{\textsf{s}}}\Bigr]-\frac{1}{\Lambda_{Q}}{\rm Tr}\,\Bigl(\overline{\textsf{q}}_{2}M_{22}\textsf{q}_{2}\Bigr)-{\cal W}_{MN}+{\cal W}_{M},

{\cal W}_{MN}=\frac{1}{\Lambda_{Q}}{\rm Tr}\,\Bigl(M_{11}N_{11}+M_{21}N_{12}+N_{21}M_{12}+M_{22}N_{21}\frac{1}{N_{11}}N_{12}\Bigr)\,,

where the nions (dual pions)
N_{11}
originate from higgsing of

{\overline{q}}^{1},q_{1}
dual quarks while
\overline{\textsf{q}}_{2},\textsf{q}_{2}

are the active quarks
{\overline{q}}^{2},q_{2}
with not higgsed colors,

\overline{\textsf{s}}
is the field strength of not higgsed dual gluons
and the hybrid nions
N_{12}
and
N_{21}
are, in essence, the
quarks
{\overline{q}}^{2},q_{2}
with higgsed colors,
{\cal W}_{M}
is given in
(
14.2.2
). The lower energy theory at
\mu<{\overline{\mu}}_{{\rm gl},\,1}
has

{\overline{N}}_{c}^{\,\prime}={\overline{N}}_{c}-n_{1}
colors and
n_{2}>N_{c}
flavors,

\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}^{\,\prime}=\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}-2n_{1}<\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}
. We consider here only the case

\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}^{\,\prime}>0
when it remains in the conformal window. In
this case the value of the pole mass
\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,2}
in this
lower energy theory is

\displaystyle\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,2}\sim\frac{\langle M_{2}\rangle}{\Lambda_{Q}}\frac{1}{z_{q}(\Lambda_{Q},{\overline{\mu}}_{{\rm gl},\,1})z^{\,\prime}_{q}({\overline{\mu}}_{{\rm gl},\,1},\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,2})}\sim{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(\rm br2)}\,,\hskip 11.74988ptz^{\,\prime}_{q}(\overline{\mu}_{{\rm gl},\,1},\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,2})\sim\Bigl(\frac{\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,2}}{{\overline{\mu}}_{{\rm gl},\,1}}\Bigr)^{\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}^{\,\prime}/n_{2}}\ll 1\,.

(21.2.5)

The fields
N_{11},N_{12},N_{21}
and
M_{11},M_{12},M_{21}

are frozen and do not evolve at
\mu<{\overline{\mu}}_{{\rm gl},\,1}
. After integrating
out remained not higgsed quarks
\overline{\textsf{q}}_{2},{\textsf{q}}_{2}

as heavy ones and not higgsed gluons at
\mu<{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(\rm br2)}

the Lagrangian of mions and nions looks as, see (
21.2.4
),

K=z_{M}(\Lambda_{Q},{\overline{\mu}}_{{\rm gl},\,1}){\rm Tr}\,K_{M}+z_{q}(\Lambda_{Q},{\overline{\mu}}_{{\rm gl},\,1})\Bigl[\,2\sqrt{N_{11}^{\dagger}N_{11}}+K_{\rm hybr}\,\Bigr],\,\,z^{\,\prime}_{M}({\overline{\mu}}_{{\rm gl},\,1},\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,2})\sim\Bigl(\frac{{\overline{\mu}}_{{\rm gl},\,1}}{\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,2}}\Bigr)^{\frac{2\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}^{\,\prime}}{n_{2}}}\gg 1,

\displaystyle K_{M}=\frac{1}{\Lambda_{Q}^{2}}\Bigl(M_{11}^{\dagger}M_{11}+M_{12}^{\dagger}M_{12}+M_{21}^{\dagger}M_{21}+z^{\,\prime}_{M}({\overline{\mu}}_{{\rm gl},\,1},\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,2})M_{22}^{\dagger}M_{22}\Bigr)\,,

(21.2.6)

{\cal W}={\overline{N}}_{c}^{\,\prime}S-{\cal W}_{MN}+{\cal W}_{M}\,,\hskip 11.74988ptS=\Bigl({\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(\rm br2)}\Bigr)^{3}\Biggl(\det\frac{\langle N_{1}\rangle}{N_{11}}\det\frac{M_{22}}{\langle M_{2}\rangle}\Biggr)^{1/{\overline{N}}_{c}^{\,\prime}},\hskip 11.74988pt{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(\rm br2)}\sim\Bigl(m_{Q}\langle M_{1}\rangle\Bigr)^{1/3}.

From (
21.2.6
), the ”masses” of mions look as

\displaystyle\mu(M_{11})\sim\mu(M_{12})\sim\mu(M_{21})\sim\frac{\Lambda_{Q}^{2}}{z_{M}(\Lambda_{Q},{\overline{\mu}}_{{\rm gl},\,1})\mu_{\Phi}}\sim\Bigl(\frac{m_{1}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\Bigr){\overline{\mu}}_{{\rm gl},\,1}\gg{\overline{\mu}}_{{\rm gl},\,1}\,,

(21.2.7)

\displaystyle\mu(M_{22})\sim\frac{\Lambda_{Q}^{2}}{z_{M}(\Lambda_{Q},{\overline{\mu}}_{{\rm gl},\,1})z^{\prime}_{M}({\overline{\mu}}_{{\rm gl},\,1},\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,2})\mu_{\Phi}}\sim\Bigl(\frac{m_{1}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\Bigr)^{\frac{3N_{c}-n_{2}}{3(n_{2}-N_{c})}}\,{\overline{\mu}}_{{\rm gl},\,1}\gg{\overline{\mu}}_{{\rm gl},\,1}\,,

(21.2.8)

while the pole masses of nions
N_{11}
are

\displaystyle\mu^{\rm pole}(N_{11})\sim\frac{\mu_{\Phi}\langle N_{1}\rangle_{\rm br2}}{z_{q}(\Lambda_{Q},{\overline{\mu}}_{{\rm gl},\,1})\Lambda_{Q}^{2}}\sim\Bigl(\frac{\mu_{\Phi}}{m_{1}}\Bigr){\overline{\mu}}_{{\rm gl},\,1}\,,

(21.2.9)

and the hybrid nions
N_{12},N_{21}
are massless,

\mu(N_{12})=\mu(N_{21})=0
. The mion ”masses”
(
21.2.7
),(
21.2.8
) are not the pole masses but simply
the low energy values of mass terms in their propagators, the
only pole masses are given in (
21.2.2
).

21.3

\rm br2
vacua,
\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}/N_{F}\ll 1

Instead of (
21.2.2
), the pole mass of mions is
parametrically smaller now,

\displaystyle\mu^{\rm pole}(M)=\frac{Z_{q}^{\,2}\Lambda_{Q}^{2}/\mu_{\Phi}}{z_{M}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu^{\rm pole}(M))}\sim Z_{q}^{\,2}\Lambda_{Q}\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\Bigr)^{\frac{N_{F}}{3(2N_{c}-N_{F})}},\hskip 11.74988pt\frac{\mu^{\rm pole}(M)}{\mu^{\rm pole}_{2}(\Phi)}\sim Z_{q}^{\,2}\ll 1\,,

(21.3.1)

while instead of (
21.2.3
) we have now

\displaystyle\mu_{q,2}=\frac{\langle M_{2}\rangle}{Z_{q}\Lambda_{Q}}\sim\frac{m_{Q}\mu_{\Phi}}{Z_{q}\Lambda_{Q}},\hskip 11.74988pt{\tilde{\mu}}^{\rm pole}_{q,2}\sim\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{Z_{q}}\Bigl(\frac{m_{Q}\mu_{\Phi}}{\Lambda_{Q}^{2}}\Bigr)^{N_{F}/3{\overline{N}}_{c}}\gg\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,1}\,,\hskip 11.74988ptZ_{q}\sim\exp\Bigl\{\frac{-{\overline{N}}_{c}}{7\,\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}}\Bigr\}\ll 1\,.

(21.3.2)

\displaystyle{\overline{\mu}}_{\rm gl,1}\sim\Lambda_{Q}\Bigl(\frac{\langle N_{1}\rangle}{\Lambda_{Q}^{2}}\Bigr)^{N_{F}/3N_{c}}\sim Z_{q}^{1/2}\Lambda_{Q}\Bigl(\frac{m_{Q}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{N_{F}/3N_{c}}\gg{\overline{\mu}}_{\rm gl,2}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\frac{{\overline{\mu}}_{\rm gl,1}}{m^{\rm pole}_{Q,1}}\sim Z_{q}^{1/2}\ll 1\,,

(21.3.3)

\displaystyle\hskip 11.74988pt\frac{{\overline{\mu}}_{\rm gl,1}}{{\tilde{\mu}}^{\rm pole}_{q,2}}\sim Z_{q}^{3/2}\Bigl(\frac{m_{1}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\Bigr)^{N_{F}/3{\overline{N}}_{c}}\gg 1\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\Lambda_{Q}\ll{\mu_{\Phi}}\ll Z_{q}^{\,3/2}m_{1}\,,\hskip 11.74988ptZ_{q}\sim\exp\{-\frac{{\overline{N}}_{c}}{7\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}}\}\ll 1\,.

(21.3.4)

Hence, at the condition (
21.3.4
), the largest mass is

{\overline{\mu}}_{\rm gl,1}
and the overall phase is also

Higgs_{1}-HQ_{2}
. But now, at
\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}/{\overline{N}}_{c}\ll 1
, it looks unnatural
to require
\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}^{\,\prime}=(\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}-2n_{1})>0
. Therefore, with

n_{1}/{\overline{N}}_{c}=O(1)
, the lower energy theory at
\mu<{\overline{\mu}}_{\rm gl,1}
has
\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}^{\,\prime}<0
and is in the logarithmic IR free
regime in the range of scales
\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,2}<\mu<{\overline{\mu}}_{\rm gl,1}
.
Then instead of (
21.2.5
) (ignoring all logarithmic
renormalization factors),

\displaystyle{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(\rm br2)}\ll\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,2}\sim\frac{\langle M_{2}\rangle_{\rm br2}}{Z_{q}\Lambda_{Q}}\frac{1}{z_{q}(\Lambda_{Q},{\overline{\mu}}_{{\rm gl},\,1})}\sim\frac{\mu_{\Phi}}{Z_{q}^{3/2}m_{1}}\,{\overline{\mu}}_{\rm gl,1}\ll{\overline{\mu}}_{\rm gl,1}\,.

(21.3.5)

The Lagrangian of mions and nions has now the form (
21.2.6
)
with accounting additionally for
Z_{q}
factors, and with a
replacement
z^{\,\prime}_{M}({\overline{\mu}}_{{\rm gl},\,1},\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,2})\sim 1
, and so

\mu(M_{22})\sim\mu(M_{11})\sim\mu(M_{12})\sim\mu(M_{21})
now,
see (
21.2.7
),(
21.2.8
),(
21.3.4
),

\displaystyle\mu(M^{i}_{j})\sim\frac{Z_{q}^{2}\Lambda_{Q}^{2}}{z_{M}(\Lambda_{Q},{\overline{\mu}}_{{\rm gl},\,1})\mu_{\Phi}}\sim Z_{q}^{3/2}\Bigl(\frac{m_{1}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\Bigr){\overline{\mu}}_{{\rm gl},\,1}\gg{\overline{\mu}}_{{\rm gl},\,1}\,,

(21.3.6)

while, instead of (
21.2.9
), the mass of nions looks now as

\displaystyle\mu^{\rm pole}(N_{11})\sim\frac{\mu_{\Phi}\langle N_{1}\rangle_{\rm br2}}{z_{q}(\Lambda_{Q},{\overline{\mu}}_{{\rm gl},\,1})\Lambda_{Q}^{2}}\sim Z_{q}^{1/2}\Bigl(\frac{\mu_{\Phi}}{m_{1}}\Bigr)\,{\overline{\mu}}_{{\rm gl},\,1}\,.

(21.3.7)

On the whole for the mass spectra in this case. a) The heaviest
are
N_{F}^{2}
mions with the pole masses (
21.3.1
) (the
’masses’ (
21.3.6
) are not the pole masses but simply the
low energy values of mass terms in the mion propagators).   b)
The next are the masses (
21.3.3
) of
n_{1}(2{\overline{N}}_{c}-n_{1})

higgsed gluons and their superpartners.   c) There is a large
number of flavored hadrons, mesons and baryons, made of
non-relativistic and weakly confined (the string tension is

\sqrt{\sigma}\sim{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(\rm br2)}\ll\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,2}
) quarks
\textsf{q}^{2},\textsf{q}_{2}
with not higgsed colors. The mass spectrum of low-lying
flavored mesons is Coulomb-like with parametrically small mass
differences,
\Delta\mu_{H}/\mu_{H}=O(\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}^{\,2}/N^{2}_{F})\ll 1
.  d) A
large number of gluonia made of
SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-n_{1})
gluons with the
mass scale
\sim{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(\rm br2)}
.  e)
n_{1}^{2}
nions
N_{11}

with the masses (
21.3.7
).  f) The hybrid nions
N_{12},N_{21}
are the Nambu-Goldstone particles here and are
massless.

22

Direct theory. Broken flavor symmetry,

\mathbf{m_{1}\ll\mu_{\Phi}\ll\frac{\Lambda_{Q}^{2}}{m_{Q}}}

22.1

\rm br1
vacua

The values of quark condensates are here

\displaystyle\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{\rm br1}\approx\frac{N_{c}}{N_{c}-n_{1}}\,m_{Q}\mu_{\Phi}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{\rm br1}\sim\Lambda_{Q}^{2}\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\Bigr)^{\frac{n_{1}}{N_{c}-n_{1}}}\Bigl(\frac{m_{Q}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\frac{n_{2}-N_{c}}{N_{c}-n_{1}}}\,,

(22.1.1)

\frac{\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{\rm br1}}{\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{\rm br1}}\sim\Bigl(\frac{m_{1}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\Bigr)^{\frac{N_{c}}{N_{c}-n_{1}}}\ll 1\,.

From these, the values of some potentially relevant masses
look as

\mu_{\rm gl,1}^{2}\sim\Bigl(a_{*}\sim 1\Bigr)z_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu_{\rm gl,1})\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{\rm br1}\,,\hskip 11.74988ptz_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu_{\rm gl,1})\sim\Bigl(\frac{\mu_{\rm gl,1}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\rm{b_{o}}/N_{F}}\,,

\displaystyle\mu_{\rm gl,1}\sim\Lambda_{Q}\Bigl(\frac{m_{Q}\mu_{\Phi}}{\Lambda_{Q}^{2}}\Bigr)^{N_{F}/3{\overline{N}}_{c}}\gg\mu_{\rm gl,2}\,,

(22.1.2)

\langle m^{\rm tot}_{Q,2}\rangle=\frac{\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{\rm br1}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\sim m_{Q}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt{\tilde{m}}^{\rm pole}_{Q,2}=\frac{\langle m^{\rm tot}_{Q,2}\rangle_{\rm br1}}{z_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q},{\tilde{m}}^{\rm pole}_{Q,2})}\,,

\displaystyle{\tilde{m}}^{\rm pole}_{Q,2}\sim\Lambda_{Q}\Bigl(\frac{m_{Q}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{N_{F}/3N_{c}}\gg m^{\rm pole}_{Q,1},\hskip 11.74988pt\,\,\,\frac{{\tilde{m}}^{\rm pole}_{Q,2}}{\mu_{\rm gl,1}}\sim\Bigl(\frac{m_{1}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\Bigr)^{N_{F}/3{\overline{N}}_{c}}\ll 1\,.

(22.1.3)

Hence, the largest mass is
\mu_{\rm gl,1}
due to higgsing of
{\overline{Q}}_{1},Q^{1}
quarks and the overall phase is
Higgs_{1}-HQ_{2}
.

The lower energy theory at
\mu<\mu_{\rm gl,1}
has
N_{c}^{\prime}=N_{c}-n_{1}

colors and
n_{2}\geq N_{f}/2
flavors. At
2n_{1}<\rm{b_{o}}
it remains in
the conformal window with
{\rm b}_{o}^{\prime}>0
, while at

2n_{1}>\rm{b_{o}},\,\,{\rm b}_{o}^{\prime}<0
it enters the logarithmic IR
free perturbative regime.

We start with
\rm{b_{o}}^{\prime}>0
. Then the value of the pole mass of
quarks
\overline{\textsf{Q}}_{2},\,\textsf{Q}^{2}
with not higgsed colors looks as

m^{\rm pole}_{\textsf{Q},2}=\frac{\langle m^{\rm tot}_{Q,2}\rangle_{\rm br1}}{z_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu_{\rm gl,1})z_{Q}^{\,\prime}(\mu_{\rm gl,1},m^{\rm pole}_{\textsf{Q},2})}\,,\hskip 11.74988ptz_{Q}^{\,\prime}(\mu_{\rm gl,1},m^{\rm pole}_{\textsf{Q},2})\sim\Bigl(\frac{m^{\rm pole}_{\textsf{Q},2}}{\mu_{\rm gl,1}}\Bigr)^{{\rm b}_{o}^{\prime}/n_{2}}\,,

\displaystyle m^{\rm pole}_{\textsf{Q},2}\sim\Lambda_{Q}\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\Bigr)^{\frac{n_{1}}{3(N_{c}-n_{1})}}\Bigl(\frac{m_{Q}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\frac{n_{2}-n_{1}}{3(N_{c}-n_{1})}}\sim{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(\rm br1)}\,.

(22.1.4)

It is technically convenient to retain all fion fields
\Phi

although, in essence, they are too heavy and dynamically
irrelevant at
\mu_{\Phi}\gg m_{1}
. After integrating out all heavy
higgsed gluons and quarks
{\overline{Q}}_{1},Q^{1}
, we write the
Lagrangian at
\mu=\mu_{\rm gl,1}
in the form

K=\Bigl[\,z_{\Phi}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu_{\rm gl,1}){\rm Tr}(\Phi^{\dagger}\Phi)+z_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu^{2}_{\rm gl,1})\Bigl(K_{{\textsf{Q}}_{2}}+K_{\Pi}\Bigr)\,\Bigr],\hskip 11.74988ptz_{\Phi}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu_{\rm gl,1})=1/z^{2}_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu_{\rm gl,1})\,,

\displaystyle K_{{\textsf{Q}}_{2}}={\rm Tr}\Bigl({\textsf{Q}}^{\dagger}_{2}{\textsf{Q}}^{2}+({\textsf{Q}}^{2}\rightarrow{\overline{\textsf{Q}}}_{2})\Bigr)\,,\hskip 11.74988ptK_{\Pi}=2{\rm Tr}\sqrt{\Pi^{\dagger}_{11}\Pi_{11}}+K_{\rm hybr},

(22.1.5)

K_{\rm hybr}={\rm Tr}\Biggl(\Pi^{\dagger}_{12}\frac{1}{\sqrt{\Pi_{11}\Pi^{\dagger}_{11}}}\Pi_{12}+\Pi_{21}\frac{1}{\sqrt{\Pi^{\dagger}_{11}\Pi_{11}}}\Pi^{\dagger}_{21}\Biggr),

{\cal W}=\Bigl[\frac{2\pi}{\alpha(\mu_{\rm gl,1})}{\textsf{S}}\Bigr]+\frac{\mu_{\Phi}}{2}\Biggl[{\rm Tr}\,(\Phi^{2})-\frac{1}{{\overline{N}}_{c}}\Bigl({\rm Tr}\,\Phi\Bigr)^{2}\Biggr]+{\rm Tr}\Bigl({\overline{\textsf{Q}}_{2}}m^{\rm tot}_{{\textsf{Q}}_{2}}{\textsf{Q}}^{2}\Bigr)+{\cal W}_{\Pi},

{\cal W}_{\Pi}={\rm Tr}\Bigl(m_{Q}\Pi_{11}+m^{\rm tot}_{{\textsf{Q}}_{2}}\,\Pi_{21}\frac{1}{\Pi_{11}}\Pi_{12}\Bigr)-{\rm Tr}\Bigl(\Phi_{11}\Pi_{11}+\Phi_{12}\Pi_{21}+\Phi_{21}\Pi_{12}\Bigr),\hskip 11.74988ptm^{\rm tot}_{{\textsf{Q}}_{2}}=(m_{Q}-\Phi_{22}),

In (
22.1.5
):
\overline{\textsf{Q}}_{2},\,\textsf{Q}^{2}
and
V
are the active

{\overline{Q}}_{2},Q^{2}
quarks and gluons with not higgsed colors
(
S
is their field strength squared),
\Pi_{12},\Pi_{21}
are the hybrid pions (in essence, these are the quarks

{\overline{Q}}_{2},Q^{2}
with higgsed colors),
z_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu^{2}_{\rm gl,1})
is the corresponding perturbative renormalization factor
of massless quarks, see (
22.1.2
), while

z_{\Phi}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu_{\rm gl,1})
is that of fions. Evolving now down in the
scale and integrating out at
\mu<{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(\rm br1)}
quarks

\overline{\textsf{Q}}_{2},\,\textsf{Q}^{2}
as heavy ones and not higgsed gluons via the
VY-procedure, we obtain the Lagrangian of pions and fions

K=\Bigl[z_{\Phi}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu_{\rm gl,1}){\rm Tr}\Bigl(\Phi^{\dagger}_{11}\Phi_{11}+\Phi^{\dagger}_{12}\Phi_{12}+\Phi^{\dagger}_{21}\Phi_{21}+z^{\,\prime}_{\Phi}(\mu_{\rm gl,1},m^{\rm pole}_{\textsf{Q},2})\Phi^{\dagger}_{22}\Phi_{22}\Bigr)+z_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu^{2}_{\rm gl,1})K_{\Pi}\Bigr],\,

\displaystyle{\cal W}=(N_{c}-n_{1})S+W_{\Phi}+W_{\Pi}\,,\hskip 11.74988ptS=\Biggl[\frac{\Lambda_{Q}^{\rm{b_{o}}}\det m^{\rm tot}_{{\textsf{Q}}_{2}}}{\det\Pi_{11}}\Biggr]^{\frac{1}{N_{c}-n_{1}}}\,,

(22.1.6)

{\cal W}_{\Phi}=\frac{\mu_{\Phi}}{2}\Biggl[{\rm Tr}(\Phi^{2})-\frac{1}{{\overline{N}}_{c}}\Bigl({\rm Tr}\,\Phi\Bigr)^{2}\Biggr],\hskip 11.74988ptz^{\,\prime}_{\Phi}(\mu_{\rm gl,1},m^{\rm pole}_{\textsf{Q},2})\sim\Bigl(\frac{\mu_{\rm gl,1}}{m^{\rm pole}_{\textsf{Q},2}}\Bigr)^{2{\rm b}_{o}^{\prime}/n_{2}}\,.

We obtain from (
22.1.6
) that all fions are heavy with the
”masses”

\displaystyle\mu(\Phi_{11})\sim\mu(\Phi_{12})\sim\mu(\Phi_{21})\sim\frac{\mu_{\Phi}}{z_{\Phi}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu_{\rm gl,1})}\sim\Bigl(\frac{\mu_{\Phi}}{m_{1}}\Bigr)^{N_{c}/{\overline{N}}_{c}}\mu_{\rm gl,1}\gg\mu_{\rm gl,1}\,,

(22.1.7)

\displaystyle\mu(\Phi_{22})\sim\frac{\mu_{\Phi}}{z_{\Phi}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu_{\rm gl,1})z^{\,\prime}_{\Phi}(\mu_{\rm gl,1},m^{\rm pole}_{\textsf{Q},2})}\sim\Bigl(\frac{\mu_{\Phi}}{m_{1}}\Bigr)^{\frac{N_{c}}{N_{c}-n_{1}}}\,m^{\rm pole}_{\textsf{Q},2}\gg m^{\rm pole}_{\textsf{Q},2}\,.

(22.1.8)

These are not the pole masses but simply the low energy values
of mass terms in their propagators. All fions are dynamically
irrelevant at all scales
\mu<\Lambda_{Q}
. The mixings of

\Phi_{12}\leftrightarrow\Pi_{12},\,\Phi_{21}\leftrightarrow\Pi_{21}
and

\Phi_{11}\leftrightarrow\Pi_{11}
are parametrically small and
neglected. We obtain then for the masses of pions
\Pi_{11}

\displaystyle\mu(\Pi_{11})\sim\Bigl(\frac{m_{1}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\Bigr)^{\frac{N_{c}(\rm{b_{o}}-2n_{1})}{3{\overline{N}}_{c}(N_{c}-n_{1})}}\,{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(\rm br1)}\sim\Bigl(\frac{m_{1}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\Bigr)^{\frac{N_{c}(\rm{b_{o}}-2n_{1})}{3{\overline{N}}_{c}(N_{c}-n_{1})}}\,m^{\rm pole}_{\textsf{Q},2}\ll m^{\rm pole}_{\textsf{Q},2}\,,

(22.1.9)

and, finally, the hybrids
\Pi_{12},\Pi_{21}
are massless,

\mu(\Pi_{12})=\mu(\Pi_{21})=0
.

At
2n_{1}>\rm{b_{o}}
the RG evolution at
m^{\rm pole}_{\textsf{Q},2}<\mu<\mu_{\rm gl,1}
is only slow
logarithmic (and is neglected). We replace then

z^{\,\prime}_{Q}(\mu_{\rm gl,1},m^{\rm pole}_{\textsf{Q},2})\sim 1
in (
22.1.4
) and

z^{\,\prime}_{\Phi}(\mu_{\rm gl,1},m^{\rm pole}_{\textsf{Q},2})\sim 1
in (
22.1.8
) and
obtain

\displaystyle\mu(\Phi_{22})\sim\mu(\Phi_{11})\sim\Bigl(\frac{\mu_{\Phi}}{m_{1}}\Bigr)^{N_{c}/{\overline{N}}_{c}}\mu_{\rm gl,1}\gg\mu_{\rm gl,1}\,,

(22.1.10)

\mu(\Pi_{11})\sim m^{\rm pole}_{\textsf{Q},2}\sim\frac{m_{Q}}{z_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu^{2}_{\rm gl,1})}\sim\Lambda_{Q}\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\Bigr)^{\rm{b_{o}}/3{\overline{N}}_{c}}\Bigl(\frac{m_{Q}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{2\,\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}/3{\overline{N}}_{c}}\sim\Bigl(\frac{m_{1}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\Bigr)^{N_{c}/{\overline{N}}_{c}}\mu_{\rm gl,1}\ll\mu_{\rm gl,1}.

\displaystyle\frac{{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(\rm br1)}}{m^{\rm pole}_{\textsf{Q},2}}\sim\Bigl(\frac{m_{1}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\Bigr)^{\Delta}\ll 1,\hskip 11.74988pt\Delta=\frac{N_{c}(2n_{1}-\rm{b_{o}})}{3{\overline{N}}_{c}(N_{c}-n_{1})}>0\,.

(22.1.11)

22.2

\rm br2
vacua

At
n_{2}<N_{c}
there are also
\rm br2
- vacua. All
their properties can be obtained by a replacement

n_{1}\leftrightarrow n_{2}
in formulas of the preceding section.
The only difference is that, because
n_{2}\geq N_{F}/2
and
so
2n_{2}>\rm{b_{o}}
, there is no analog of the conformal regime at

\mu<\mu_{\rm gl,1}
with
2n_{1}<\rm{b_{o}}
. I.e. at
\mu<\mu_{\rm gl,2}
the lower energy theory will be always in the
perturbative IR free logarithmic regime and the overall phase
will be
Higgs_{2}-HQ_{1}
.

23

Dual theory. Broken flavor symmetry,

\mathbf{m_{1}\ll\mu_{\Phi}\ll\frac{\Lambda_{Q}^{2}}{m_{Q}}}

23.1

\rm br1
vacua,
\,\,\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}/N_{F}\ll 1

We recall that condensates of mions and dual quarks in these
vacua are

\displaystyle\langle M_{1}\rangle_{\rm br1}\approx\frac{N_{c}}{N_{c}-n_{1}}\,m_{Q}\mu_{\Phi}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\langle M_{2}\rangle_{\rm br1}\sim\Lambda_{Q}^{2}\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\Bigr)^{\frac{n_{1}}{N_{c}-n_{1}}}\Bigl(\frac{m_{Q}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\frac{n_{2}-N_{c}}{N_{c}-n_{1}}}\,,

(23.1.1)

\frac{\langle M_{2}\rangle_{\rm br1}}{\langle M_{1}\rangle_{\rm br1}}\sim\Bigl(\frac{m_{1}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\Bigr)^{\frac{N_{c}}{N_{c}-n_{1}}}\ll 1\,,

\langle N_{2}\rangle_{\rm br1}\equiv\langle{\overline{q}}^{2}q_{2}(\mu=\Lambda_{Q})\rangle_{\rm br1}=Z_{q}\frac{\langle M_{1}\rangle_{\rm br1}\Lambda_{Q}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\sim Z_{q}m_{Q}\Lambda_{Q}\gg\langle N_{1}\rangle_{\rm br1}\,,

and some potentially relevant masses look here as

\displaystyle\langle({\overline{q}}q)_{1}\rangle=\langle{\overline{q}}^{1}q_{1}(\mu=\Lambda_{Q})\rangle=\frac{\langle M_{1}\rangle_{\rm br1}}{Z_{q}\Lambda_{Q}}\sim\frac{m_{Q}\mu_{\Phi}}{Z_{q}\Lambda_{Q}}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\frac{\langle{(\overline{q}}q)_{2}\rangle}{\langle({\overline{q}}q)_{1}\rangle}=\frac{\langle M_{2}\rangle_{\rm br1}}{\langle M_{1}\rangle_{\rm br1}}\ll 1\,,

(23.1.2)

Z_{q}\sim\exp\Bigl\{-\frac{1}{3{\overline{a}}_{*}}\Bigr\}\sim\exp\Bigl\{-\frac{{\overline{N}}_{c}}{7\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}}\Bigr\}\ll 1\,,

\displaystyle\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,1}\sim\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{Z_{q}}\Bigl(\frac{m_{Q}\mu_{\Phi}}{\Lambda_{Q}^{2}}\Bigr)^{N_{F}/3{\overline{N}}_{c}}\gg\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,2}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\frac{{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(\rm br1)}}{\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,1}}\sim Z_{q}\Bigl(\frac{m_{1}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\Bigr)^{\frac{n_{2}N_{c}}{3{\overline{N}}_{c}(N_{c}-n_{1})}}\ll 1\,,

(23.1.3)

{\overline{\mu}}_{{\rm gl},\,2}\sim\Lambda_{Q}\Bigl(\frac{\langle N_{2}\rangle}{\Lambda_{Q}^{2}}\Bigr)^{N_{F}/3N_{c}}\sim Z_{q}^{1/2}\Lambda_{Q}\Bigl(\frac{m_{Q}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{N_{F}/3N_{c}}\gg{\overline{\mu}}_{{\rm gl},\,1}\,,

\displaystyle\frac{{\overline{\mu}}_{{\rm gl},\,2}}{\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,1}}\sim Z_{q}^{3/2}\Bigl(\frac{m_{1}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\Bigr)^{N_{F}/3{\overline{N}}_{c}}\ll 1\,.

(23.1.4)

Hence, the largest mass is
\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,1}
while the overall phase is

HQ_{1}-HQ_{2}
. We consider below only the case
n_{1}<\rm{b_{o}}/2
, so
that the lower energy theory with
{\overline{N}}_{c}
colors and

N^{\prime}_{F}=n_{2}
flavors at
\mu<\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,1}
remains in the conformal
window.

After integrating out the heaviest quarks
{\overline{q}}^{1},q_{1}
at

\mu<\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,1}
and
{\overline{q}}^{2},q_{2}
quarks at
\mu<\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,2}
and, finally, all
SU({\overline{N}}_{c})
dual gluons at

\mu<{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(\rm br1)}
, the Lagrangian of mions looks as

\displaystyle K=\frac{z_{M}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,1})}{Z^{2}_{q}\Lambda_{Q}^{2}}\,{\rm Tr}\Bigl[\,M_{11}^{\dagger}M_{11}+M_{12}^{\dagger}M_{12}+M_{21}^{\dagger}M_{21}+z^{\,\prime}_{M}(\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,1},\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,2})M_{22}^{\dagger}M_{22}\Bigr]\,,

(23.1.5)

{\cal W}=-{\overline{N}}_{c}S+{\cal W}_{M}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\hskip 11.74988ptS=\Bigl(\frac{\det M}{\Lambda_{Q}^{\rm{b_{o}}}}\Bigr)^{1/{\overline{N}}_{c}}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(\rm br1)}=\langle S\rangle^{1/3}\sim\Bigl(m_{Q}\langle M_{2}\rangle\Bigr)^{1/3}\,.

{\cal W}_{M}=m_{Q}{\rm Tr}M-\frac{1}{2\mu_{\Phi}}\Bigl[\,{\rm Tr}(M^{2})-\frac{1}{N_{c}}({\rm Tr}M)^{2}\Bigr]\,,\hskip 11.74988ptz_{M}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,1})\sim\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,1}}\Bigr)^{2\,\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}/N_{F}}\gg 1\,.

From (
23.1.5
) : the hybrids
M_{12}
and
M_{21}
are
massless,
\mu(M_{12})=\mu(M_{21})=0
, while the pole mass of

M_{11}
is (compare with (
22.1.9
) )

\displaystyle\mu^{\rm pole}(M_{11})\sim\frac{Z^{2}_{q}\Lambda_{Q}^{2}}{z_{M}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,1})\mu_{\Phi}}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\frac{\mu^{\rm pole}(M_{11})}{{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(\rm br1)}}\sim Z^{2}_{q}\Bigl(\frac{m_{1}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\Bigr)^{\frac{N_{c}(\rm{b_{o}}-2n_{1})}{3{\overline{N}}_{c}(N_{c}-n_{1})}}\ll 1\,.

(23.1.6)

The parametric behavior of
\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,2}
and

z^{\,\prime}_{M}(\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,1},\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,2})
depends on the value

\mu_{\Phi}\lessgtr{\tilde{\mu}}_{\Phi,1}
(see below). We consider
first the case
\mu_{\Phi}\gg{\tilde{\mu}}_{\Phi,1}
so that, by
definition, the lower energy theory with
{\overline{N}}_{c}
colors and
n_{2}

flavors had enough ”time” to evolve and entered already the new
conformal regime at
\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,2}<\mu\ll\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,1}
, with
{\rm\overline{b}\,}^{\prime}_{o}/{\overline{N}}_{c}=(3{\overline{N}}_{c}-n_{2})/{\overline{N}}_{c}=O(1)
and
{\overline{a}\,}^{\prime}_{*}=O(1)
. Hence, when the quarks
{\overline{q}}^{2},q_{2}

decouple as heavy ones at
\mu<\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,2}
, the coupling
{\overline{a}}_{YM}
of the remained
SU({\overline{N}}_{c})
Yang-Mills theory is
{\overline{a}}_{YM}\sim{\overline{a}\,}^{\prime}_{*}=O(1)
and this means that

\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,2}\sim{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(\rm br1)}
. This can be obtained also in a
direct way. The running mass of quarks
{\overline{q}}^{2},q_{2}
at

\mu=\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,1}
is, see (
23.1.1
)-(
23.1.3
),

\displaystyle\mu_{q,2}(\mu=\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,1})=\frac{\langle M_{2}\rangle_{\rm br1}}{\langle M_{1}\rangle_{\rm br1}}\,\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,1}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,2}=\frac{\mu_{q,2}(\mu=\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,1})}{z^{\,\prime}_{q}(\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,1},\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,2})}\sim{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(\rm br1)}\sim\Bigl(m_{Q}\langle M_{2}\rangle\Bigr)^{1/3}\,,

(23.1.7)

z^{\,\prime}_{q}(\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,1},\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,2})=\Bigl(\frac{\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,2}}{\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,1}}\Bigr)^{\frac{{\rm\overline{b}\,}^{\prime}_{o}}{n_{2}}}\rho\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\rho=\Bigl(\frac{{\overline{a}\,}_{*}}{{\overline{a}\,}^{\prime}_{*}}\Bigr)^{\frac{{\overline{N}}_{c}}{n_{2}}}\exp\Bigl\{\frac{{\overline{N}}_{c}}{n_{2}}\Bigl(\frac{1}{{\overline{a}\,}_{*}}-\frac{1}{{\overline{a}\,}^{\prime}_{*}}\Bigr)\Bigr\}\sim\exp\Bigl\{\frac{{\overline{N}}_{c}}{n_{2}}\frac{1}{{\overline{a}\,}_{*}}\Bigr\}\gg 1\,.

We obtain from (
23.1.5
) that the main contribution to the
mass of mions
M_{22}
originates from the non-perturbative term

\sim S
in the superpotential and, using
(
23.1.5
),(
23.1.7
),

\displaystyle z^{\,\prime}_{M}(\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,1},\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,2})=\frac{{\overline{a}}_{f}(\mu=\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,1})}{{\overline{a}}_{f}(\mu=\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,2})}\Bigl(\frac{1}{z^{\,\prime}_{q}(\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,1},\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,2})}\Bigr)^{2}\sim\Bigl(\frac{1}{z^{\,\prime}_{q}(\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,1},\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,2})}\Bigr)^{2}\,,

(23.1.8)

\displaystyle\mu^{\rm pole}(M_{22})\sim\frac{Z_{q}^{2}\Lambda_{Q}^{2}}{z_{M}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,1})z^{\,\prime}_{M}(\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,1},\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,2})}\Biggl(\frac{\langle S\rangle}{\langle M_{2}\rangle^{2}}=\frac{\langle M_{1}\rangle}{\langle M_{2}\rangle}\frac{1}{\mu_{\Phi}}\Biggr)_{\rm br1}\,\,\sim{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(\rm br1)}\sim\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,2}\,.

(23.1.9)

We consider now the region
m_{1}\ll\mu_{\Phi}\ll{\tilde{\mu}}_{\Phi,1},\,2n_{1}\lessgtr\rm{b_{o}}
where, by definition,
\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,2}
is too close to

\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,1}
, so that the range of scales
\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,2}<\mu<\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,1}
is too small
and the lower energy theory at
\mu<\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,1}
has no enough ”time”
to enter a new regime (conformal at
2n_{1}<\rm{b_{o}}
or strong
coupling one at
2n_{1}>\rm{b_{o}}
) and remains in the weak coupling
logarithmic regime. Then, ignoring logarithmic effects in
renormalization factors,
z^{\,\prime}_{q}(\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,1},\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,2})\sim z^{\,\prime}_{M}(\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,1},\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,2})\sim 1
, and keeping as always only
the exponential dependence on
{\overline{N}}_{c}/\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}
:

\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,2}\sim\frac{\langle M_{2}\rangle_{\rm br1}}{\langle M_{1}\rangle_{\rm br1}}\,\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,1}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\hskip 11.74988pt\frac{{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(\rm br1)}}{\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,2}}\ll 1\hskip 11.74988pt\rightarrow\hskip 11.74988ptm_{1}\ll\mu_{\Phi}\ll{\tilde{\mu}}_{\Phi,1}\,,

\displaystyle{\tilde{\mu}}_{\Phi,1}\sim\exp\Bigl\{\frac{(N_{c}-n_{1})}{2n_{1}}\frac{1}{{\overline{a}\,}_{*}}\Bigr\}m_{1}\gg m_{1}\,.

(23.1.10)

The pole mass of mions
M_{22}
looks in this case as

\displaystyle\frac{\mu^{\rm pole}(M_{22})}{\mu^{\rm pole}(M_{11})}\sim\frac{\langle M_{1}\rangle_{\rm br1}}{\langle M_{2}\rangle_{\rm br1}}\gg 1,\hskip 11.74988pt\frac{\mu^{\rm pole}(M_{22})}{{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(\rm br1)}}\sim Z^{2}_{q}\Bigl(\frac{\mu_{\Phi}}{m_{1}}\Bigr)^{\frac{2n_{1}N_{c}}{3{\overline{N}}_{c}(N_{c}-n_{1})}}\ll 1\,.

(23.1.11)

On the whole, see (
23.1.10
), the mass spectrum at

m_{1}\ll\mu_{\Phi}\ll{\tilde{\mu}}_{\Phi,1}
and
2n_{1}\lessgtr\rm{b_{o}}
looks
as follows. a) There is a large number of heaviest hadrons made
of weakly coupled (and weakly confined, the tension of the
confining string is
\sqrt{\sigma}\sim{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(\rm br1)}\ll\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,1})

non-relativistic quarks
{\overline{q}}^{1},q_{1}
, the scale of their
masses is
\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,1}
, see (
23.1.3
).  b) The next physical
mass scale is due to
\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,2}\,:\,\,{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(\rm br1)}\ll\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,2}\ll\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,1}
. Hence, there is also a large number of
hadrons made of weakly coupled and weakly confined
non-relativistic quarks
{\overline{q}}^{2},q_{2}
, the scale of their
masses is
\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,2}
, see (
23.1.10
), and a large number of
heavy hybrid hadrons with the masses
\sim(\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,1}+\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,2})
. Because
all quarks are weakly coupled and non-relativistic in all three
flavor sectors,
"11",\,"22"
and
"12+21"
, the mass spectrum
of low-lying flavored mesons is Coulomb-like with parametrically
small mass differences
\Delta\mu_{H}/\mu_{H}=O(\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}^{2}/{\overline{N}}_{c}^{2})\ll 1
.  c) A large number of gluonia made of
SU({\overline{N}}_{c})
gluons,
with the mass scale
\sim{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(\rm br1)}\sim\Bigl(m_{Q}\langle M_{2}\rangle\Bigr)^{1/3}
, see (
23.1.1
).
d)
n^{2}_{2}
mions
M_{22}
with the pole masses
\mu^{\rm pole}(M_{22})\ll{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(\rm br1)}
, see (
23.1.11
).
e)
n^{2}_{1}
mions
M_{11}
with the pole masses
\mu^{\rm pole}(M_{11})\ll\mu^{\rm pole}(M_{22})
, see
(
23.1.6
),(
23.1.9
),(
23.1.11
).  f)
2n_{1}n_{2}

hybrids
M_{12},M_{21}
are massless,
\mu(M_{12})=\mu(M_{21})=0
.

The pole mass of quarks
{\overline{q}}^{2},q_{2}
is smaller at

{\tilde{\mu}}_{\Phi,1}\ll\mu_{\Phi}\ll\Lambda_{Q}^{2}/m_{Q}
and
2n_{1}<\rm{b_{o}}
, and
stays at
\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,2}\sim{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(\rm br1)}
, while the mass of mions

M_{22}
is larger and also stays at
\mu(M_{22})\sim{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(\rm br1)}
.

23.2

\rm br2
vacua,
\,\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}/N_{F}\ll 1

The condensates of mions look in these br2 - vacua
as in (
23.1.1
) with the exchange
1\leftrightarrow 2
. The
largest mass is
\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,2}
,

\displaystyle\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,2}\sim\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{Z_{q}}\Bigl(\frac{m_{Q}\mu_{\Phi}}{\Lambda_{Q}^{2}}\Bigr)^{N_{F}/3{\overline{N}}_{c}}\gg\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,1}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\frac{{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(\rm br2)}}{\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,2}}\sim Z_{q}\Bigl(\frac{m_{1}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\Bigr)^{\frac{n_{1}N_{c}}{3{\overline{N}}_{c}(N_{c}-n_{2})}}\ll 1\,,

(23.2.1)

and the overall phase is
HQ_{1}-HQ_{2}
. After decoupling the
heaviest quarks
{\overline{q}}^{2},q_{2}
at
\mu<\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,2}
the lower energy
theory remains in the weak coupling logarithmic regime at, see
(
23.1.10
),

\displaystyle\frac{{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(\rm br2)}}{\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,1}}\ll 1\hskip 11.74988pt\rightarrow\hskip 11.74988ptm_{1}\ll\mu_{\Phi}\ll{\tilde{\mu}}_{\Phi,2}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\frac{{\tilde{\mu}}_{\Phi,2}}{m_{1}}\sim\exp\Bigl\{\frac{(N_{c}-n_{2})}{2n_{2}}\frac{1}{{\overline{a}\,}_{*}}\Bigr\}\gg 1\,.

(23.2.2)

Hence, the mass spectra in this range
m_{1}<\mu_{\Phi}\ll{\tilde{\mu}}_{\Phi,2}
can be obtained from corresponding
formulas by the replacements
n_{1}\leftrightarrow n_{2}
.

But because
n_{2}\geq N_{F}/2
, the lower energy theory with

1<n_{1}/{\overline{N}}_{c}<3/2
is in the strong coupling regime at

\mu_{\Phi}\gg{\tilde{\mu}}_{\Phi,2}
, with
{\overline{a}}(\mu)\gg 1
at

{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(\rm br2)}\ll\mu\ll\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,2}
. We do not consider the strong
coupling regime here.

Part IIb.
\mathbf{2N_{c}<N_{F}<3N_{c}}
.

24

Introduction

Considered here in sections 24-29 is the
{\cal N}=1
SQCD-type
theory with
SU(N_{c})
colors (and their Seiberg’s dual with
SU(N_{F}-N_{c})

dual   colors) and
N_{F}
flavors of light quarks, and with
N_{F}^{2}
additional
colorless flavored fields
\Phi^{j}_{i}
(fions). But now with
N_{F}
in the
different range
2N_{c}<N_{F}<3N_{c}
. The multiplicities of various vacua,
quark and gluino condensates and mass spectra are found.

This region
2N_{c}<N_{F}<3N_{c}
was considered in
[
25
]
.
As shown in
[
25
]
, the multiplicities of various vacua and mass spectra
therein  differ even qualitatively from those in the region
3N_{c}/2<N_{F}<2N_{c}

[
17
]
.

Recall that the Lagrangian of the direct
\Phi
-theory at the scale
\mu=\Lambda_{Q}

looks as

37
37

37

The gluon exponents are always implied in the Kahler terms. Besides, here and
everywhere below in the text we neglect for simplicity all RG-evolution effects
if they are logarithmic only.

K={\rm Tr}\,\Bigl(\Phi^{\dagger}\Phi\Bigr)+{\rm Tr}\Bigl(\,Q^{\dagger}Q+(Q\rightarrow{\overline{Q}})\,\Bigr)\,,\hskip 11.74988pt{\cal W}=\frac{2\pi}{\alpha(\mu=\Lambda_{Q})}S+{\cal W}_{\rm\ matter}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt{\cal W}_{\rm matter}={\cal W}_{\Phi}+{\cal W}_{Q}\,,

\displaystyle{\cal W}_{\Phi}=\frac{\mu_{\Phi}}{2}\Biggl[{\rm Tr}\,(\Phi^{2})-\frac{1}{{\overline{N}}_{c}}\Bigl({\rm Tr}\,\Phi\Bigr)^{2}\Biggr],\,\,{\cal W}_{Q}={\rm Tr}\,({\overline{Q}}m_{Q}^{\rm tot}Q),\,\,m_{Q}^{\rm tot}=(m_{Q}-\Phi),\,\,{\overline{N}}_{c}=N_{F}-N_{c}.\hskip 11.74988pt

(24.0.1)

Here : the gauge group is
SU(N_{c})
,
\mu_{\Phi}
and
m_{Q}
are the mass
parameters,
S=W^{A}_{\beta}W^{A,\,\beta}/32\pi^{2}
, where
W^{A}_{\beta}

is the gauge field strength,
A=1...N_{c}^{2}-1,\,\beta=1,2
,
a(\mu)=N_{c}\alpha(\mu)/2\pi=N_{c}g^{2}(\mu)/8\pi^{2}
is the gauge coupling with its scale factor

\Lambda_{Q}
.

Besides, in addition to the direct
\Phi
-theory in (
24.0.1
), we
calculate all this also   in Seiberg’s dual variant
[
5
,
2
]
. The
Lagrangian of the dual
d\Phi
-theory at the scale
\mu=\Lambda_{Q}
looks as

K={\rm Tr}\,(\Phi^{\dagger}\Phi)+{\rm Tr}\Bigl(q^{\dagger}q+(q\rightarrow\overline{q})\,\Bigr)+{\rm Tr}\,\Bigl(\frac{M^{\dagger}M}{\Lambda_{Q}^{2}}\Bigr)\,,\hskip 11.74988pt{\cal W}=\,\,\frac{2\pi}{\tilde{\alpha}(\mu=\Lambda_{Q})}\,{\widetilde{S}}+{\cal W}_{\Phi}+{\cal W}_{q}\,,

\displaystyle{\cal W}_{q}={\rm Tr}\,\Bigl(M(m_{Q}-\Phi)\Bigr)-\frac{1}{\Lambda_{Q}}\,\rm{Tr}\Bigl({\overline{q}}\,M\,q\Bigr)\,,

(24.0.2)

Here :  the number of dual colors is
{\overline{N}}_{c}=(N_{F}-N_{c})
and
M^{i}_{j}\rightarrow({\overline{Q}}_{j}Q^{i})
are the
N_{F}^{2}
elementary mion fields,
{\tilde{a}}(\mu)={\overline{N}}_{c}{\tilde{\alpha}}(\mu)/2\pi={\overline{N}}_{c}{\tilde{g}}^{2}(\mu)/8\pi^{2}
is the dual gauge coupling (with
its scale parameter
\Lambda_{q}=-\Lambda_{Q}
),
{\widetilde{S}}={\widetilde{W}}^{B}_{\beta}\,{\widetilde{W}}^{B,\,\beta}/32\pi^{2}
,
{\widetilde{W}}^{B}_{\beta}
is the dual gluon field
strength,
B=1...{\overline{N}}_{c}^{2}-1
. The gluino condensates of the direct and dual
theories are matched as well as
\langle M^{i}_{j}\rangle
and
\langle{\overline{Q}}_{j}Q^{i}\rangle\equiv\sum_{a=1}^{N_{c}}\langle{\overline{Q}}^{\,a}_{j}Q^{i}_{a}\rangle
,

\displaystyle\langle{-\,\widetilde{S}}\rangle=\langle S\rangle,\,\,\langle M^{i}_{j}\rangle\equiv\langle M^{i}_{j}(\mu=\Lambda_{Q})\rangle=\delta^{i}_{j}\langle M_{i}\rangle=\langle{\overline{Q}}_{j}Q^{i}\rangle\equiv\langle{\overline{Q}}_{j}Q^{i}(\mu=\Lambda_{Q})\rangle=\delta^{i}_{j}\langle(({\overline{Q}}Q))_{i}\rangle.\,\,\,

(24.0.3)

In sections 25-29 the hierarchies of parameters entering
(
24.0.1
),(
24.0.2
) are
m_{Q}\ll\Lambda_{Q}\ll\mu_{\Phi}
,

\mu_{\Phi}
is varied while
m_{Q}
and
\Lambda_{Q}
stay intact.

In those cases when the fields
\Phi
are too heavy and
dynamically irrelevant, they can be integrated out and the
matter Lagrangians of the direct and dual theories take the form

\displaystyle K={\rm Tr}\Bigl(\,Q^{\dagger}Q+(Q\rightarrow{\overline{Q}})\,\Bigr),\,\,W_{Q}=m_{Q}{\rm Tr}({\overline{Q}}Q)-\frac{1}{2\mu_{\Phi}}\Biggl(\sum_{i,j}\,({\overline{Q}}_{j}Q^{i})({\overline{Q}}_{i}Q^{j})-\frac{1}{N_{c}}\Bigl({\rm Tr}\,{\overline{Q}}Q\Bigr)^{2}\Biggr),

(24.0.4)

\displaystyle K={\rm Tr}\Bigl(q^{\dagger}q+(q\rightarrow\overline{q})\,\Bigr)+{\rm Tr}\,\frac{M^{\dagger}M}{\Lambda_{Q}^{2}}\,,

(24.0.5)

{\cal W}_{\rm matter}={\cal W}_{M}-\frac{1}{\Lambda_{Q}}\,\rm{Tr}\Bigl({\overline{q}}\,M\,q\Bigr)\,,\hskip 11.74988pt{\cal W}_{M}=m_{Q}\rm{Tr}\,M-\frac{1}{2\mu_{\Phi}}\Biggl[{\rm Tr}\,(M^{2})-\frac{1}{N_{c}}({\rm Tr}\,M)^{2}\Biggr].

It was shown in
[
25
]
that in all considered in sections 25-29 vacua of
both the direct and dual theories quarks are either in the weak coupling or
conformal regimes with the coupling constants
\lesssim 1
. The dynamics of
these regimes is sufficiently simple and well understood, so that there was

no real need to use for calculations of mass spectra the dynamical
scenario,
introduced in

[
20
]
.

38
38

38

In those cases when the coupling is small and in the case of conformal regime
this scenario is literally standard. The use of this scenario is really needed
only for calculations of mass spectra in the regions of
parametrically large coupling
a\gg 1
. Besides, it is worth noting that the
appearance of additional parametrically light composite solitons will influence
the ’t Hooft triangles.

As shown in sections 25-29, many properties of both direct

\Phi
-theories and dual
d\Phi
-theories at
2N_{c}<N_{F}<3N_{c}
,
i.e. multiplicities of vacua and mass spectra differ from those
calculated in
[
17
]
for
3N_{c}/2<N_{F}<2N_{c}
.

25

Quark condensates and multiplicities of vacua at
\mathbf{N_{F}>2N_{c}}

For the reader convenience, we reproduce here first some useful
formulas for the theories (
24.0.1
),(
24.0.2
).

The Konishi anomalies
[
14
]
for the
i
-th flavor look in the direct

\Phi
-theory as

\displaystyle\langle\Phi^{j}_{i}\rangle\langle\frac{\partial W_{\Phi}}{\partial\Phi^{j}_{i}}\rangle=0\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\langle m_{Q,i}^{\rm tot}\rangle\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{i}\rangle=\langle S\rangle\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\langle\Phi^{j}_{i}\rangle=\delta^{j}_{i}\langle\Phi_{i}\rangle,\hskip 11.74988pt\langle m_{Q,\,i}^{\rm tot}\rangle=m_{Q}-\langle\Phi_{i}\rangle\,,

(25.0.1)

\langle\Phi_{j}^{i}\rangle=\frac{1}{\mu_{\Phi}}\Biggl(\langle{\overline{Q}}_{j}Q^{i}\rangle-\delta^{i}_{j}\frac{1}{N_{c}}{\rm Tr}\,\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)\rangle\Biggr),\,\,\langle{\overline{Q}}_{j}Q^{i}\rangle\equiv\sum_{a=1}^{N_{c}}\langle{\overline{Q}}^{\,a}_{j}Q^{i}_{a}\rangle=\delta^{i}_{j}\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{i}\rangle,\,\,{\it i,j}=1\,...\,N_{F}\,,

and
\langle m_{Q,i}^{\rm tot}\rangle
is the value of the quark running mass
at   the scale
\mu=\Lambda_{Q}
.

The values of the quark condensates for the
i
-th flavor,
\langle{\overline{Q}}_{i}Q^{i}\rangle
, in various vacua can be obtained from the standard effective
superpotential
W^{\rm eff}_{\rm tot}(\Pi)
depending only on quark bilinears

\Pi^{i}_{j}=({\overline{Q}}_{j}Q^{i})
. ( It is worth recalling that this is
not
a
genuine low energy superpotential, (
25.0.3
) can be used
only
for
finding the values of mean vacuum values
\langle{\overline{Q}}_{j}Q^{i}\rangle
. The
genuine low energy superpotentials in each vacuum are given below in the text
).

\displaystyle W^{\rm eff}_{\rm tot}(\Pi)=W_{Q}-{\overline{N}}_{c}S,\,\,S=\Bigl(\frac{\det{\overline{Q}}Q}{\Lambda_{Q}^{\rm{b_{o}}}}\Bigr)^{1/{\overline{N}}_{c}}\equiv{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{3},\,\,\rm{b_{o}}=3N_{c}-N_{F}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt{\overline{N}}_{c}=N_{F}-N_{c}\,.

(25.0.2)

Because
\langle{\overline{Q}}_{j}Q^{i}\rangle
is holomorphic in
\mu_{\Phi}
, the values of

\langle{\overline{Q}}_{j}Q^{i}\rangle
obtained in a standard way from (
25.0.2
)
can be used both at large and small values of
\mu_{\Phi}
.

For the vacua with the spontaneously broken flavor symmetry,
U(N_{F})\rightarrow U(n_{1})\times U(n_{2}),\,\,1\leq n_{1}\leq N_{F}/2,\,\,n_{2}\geq N_{F}/2
, the most
convenient way to find the quark condensates is to use the relations following
from (
25.0.2
):

\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}+({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}-\frac{1}{N_{c}}{\rm Tr}\,({\overline{Q}}Q)\,\rangle_{\rm br}=m_{Q}\mu_{\Phi},\hskip 11.74988pt\langle S\rangle_{\rm br}=\Bigl(\frac{\det\langle{\overline{Q}}Q\rangle_{\rm br}}{\Lambda_{Q}^{\rm{b_{o}}}}\Bigr)^{1/{\overline{N}}_{c}}=\frac{\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{\rm br}\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{\rm br}}{\mu_{\Phi}},

\displaystyle\det\langle{\overline{Q}}Q\rangle_{\rm br}=\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle^{{\rm n}_{1}}_{\rm br}\,\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle^{{\rm n}_{2}}_{\rm br}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\equiv\sum_{a=1}^{N_{c}}{\overline{Q}}^{\,a}_{1}Q^{1}_{a}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\equiv\sum_{a=1}^{N_{c}}{\overline{Q}}^{\,a}_{2}Q^{2}_{a}\,,

(25.0.3)

\langle m^{\rm tot}_{Q,1}\rangle_{\rm br}=m_{Q}-\langle\Phi_{1}\rangle_{\rm br}=\frac{\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{\rm br}}{\mu_{\Phi}},\hskip 11.74988pt\langle m^{\rm tot}_{Q,2}\rangle_{\rm br}=m_{Q}-\langle\Phi_{2}\rangle_{\rm br}=\frac{\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{\rm br}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\,.

\mu_{\Phi}\lessgtr m_{1}

while the br1 - vacua with

n_{1}>N_{c}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle\sim m_{Q}\mu_{\Phi}\gg\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle\hskip 11.74988pt{\rm at}\hskip 11.74988pt\mu_{\Phi}\ll m_{1}

\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle\sim\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle\sim\Lambda_{Q}^{2}\,(\mu_{\Phi}/\Lambda_{Q})^{{\overline{N}}_{c}/(N_{F}-2N_{c})}\hskip 11.74988pt{\rm at}\hskip 11.74988pt\mu_{\Phi}\gg m_{1}\,,

etc. The exceptions are the special vacua with
n_{1}=N_{c}\,,\,n_{2}={\overline{N}}_{c}
. In
these, the parametric behavior

\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle\sim m_{Q}\mu_{\Phi}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle\sim\Lambda_{Q}^{2}\,(\mu_{\Phi}/\Lambda_{Q})^{{\overline{N}}_{c}/(N_{F}-2N_{c})}

remains the same but the hierarchy is reversed at
\mu_{\Phi}\lessgtr m_{1}
:

\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle/\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle\sim(\mu_{\Phi}/m_{1})^{N_{c}/(N_{F}-2N_{c})}\,.

\displaystyle N^{\rm tot}_{\rm br}(n_{1})=\Bigl[(N_{F}-2N_{c})+\theta(N_{c}-n_{1})(N_{c}-n_{1})\Bigr]{\overline{C}}^{\,\rm n_{1}}_{N_{F}},\hskip 11.74988ptN^{\rm tot}_{\rm br}=\sum_{n_{1}=1}^{[N_{F}/2]}N^{\rm tot}_{\rm br}(n_{1})

Direct theory. Unbroken flavor symmetry.

\mathbf{\hskip 28.45274pt2N_{c}<N_{F}<3N_{c}}

\beta
-functions

Because
m_{1}=\Lambda_{Q}(m_{Q}/\Lambda_{Q})^{(N_{F}-2N_{c})/N_{c}}\ll\Lambda_{Q}
at
m_{Q}\ll\Lambda_{Q}
while

\mu_{\Phi}\gg\Lambda_{Q}

There are
(N_{F}-2N_{c})
L - vacua (
25.1.2
) with the quark condensates

\displaystyle\frac{\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)\rangle_{L}}{\Lambda_{Q}^{2}}\sim\Biggl(\frac{\mu_{\Phi}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Biggr)^{\frac{{\overline{N}}_{c}}{N_{F}-2N_{c}}}\gg 1\,.

(26.0.1)

So, the gluon masses due to possible higgsing of some quark flavors are large
(neglecting here and below in this section all logarithmic factors due to the
RG-evolution),

\displaystyle\mu^{\rm pole}_{\rm gl,L}\sim\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)\rangle_{L}^{1/2}\gg\Lambda_{Q}.

(26.0.2)

But the quark masses are even larger,

\displaystyle m_{Q,\,L}^{\rm pole}\sim\langle m_{Q}^{\rm tot}\rangle_{L}\sim\langle\Phi\rangle_{L}\sim\frac{\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)\rangle_{L}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\sim\Lambda_{Q}\Bigl(\frac{\mu_{\Phi}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\frac{N_{c}}{N_{F}-2N_{c}}}\gg\Lambda_{Q},\,\,\frac{\mu^{\rm pole}_{\rm gl,\,L}}{m_{Q,\,L}^{\rm\,pole}}\sim\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\Bigr)^{\frac{3N_{c}-N_{F}}{2(N_{F}-2N_{c})}}\ll 1.\hskip 11.74988pt\,\,

(26.0.3)

Therefore, all quarks are in the HQ (heavy quark) phase and are not higgsed but
confined. This is self-consistent in these vacua with the unbroken global

SU(N_{F}>N_{c})
symmetry, as otherwise higgsed quarks
\langle Q^{i}_{a}\rangle\neq 0

will break spontaneously
SU(N_{F})
due to the rank restriction.

After integrating out all quarks as heavy ones at scales
\mu<m^{\rm pole}_{Q},\,\,m^{\rm pole}_{Q}\gg\Lambda_{Q}

in the weak coupling regime
, there
remain
N_{F}^{2}
fions
\Phi
and the
SU(N_{c})
SYM with the scale factor of its
gauge coupling

\displaystyle\langle{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(L)}\rangle=\Bigl(\Lambda_{Q}^{\rm{b_{o}}}\det\langle m^{\rm tot}_{Q}\rangle\Bigr)^{1/3N_{c}}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\frac{\langle{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(L)}\rangle}{\Lambda_{Q}}\sim\Bigl(\frac{\mu_{\Phi}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\frac{N_{F}}{3(N_{F}-2N_{c})}}\ll\frac{m_{Q,\,L}^{\rm pole}}{\Lambda_{Q}}.

(26.0.4)

After integrating out all gluons at the scale
\mu<\langle{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(L)}\rangle

through the Veneziano-Yankielowics (VY) procedure
[
15
]
, the Lagrangian
of
N_{F}^{2}
fions looks as, see (
24.0.1
) for
{\cal W}_{\Phi}
,
for
{\cal W}_{\Phi}
,

\displaystyle K_{\Phi}\sim{\rm Tr}\,(\Phi^{\dagger}\Phi),\,\,{\cal W}=N_{c}\Bigl(\Lambda_{Q}^{\rm{b_{o}}}\det m^{\rm tot}_{Q,\,L}\Bigr)^{1/N_{c}}+{\cal W}_{\Phi}\,,\hskip 11.74988ptm^{\rm tot}_{Q}=m_{Q}-\Phi,\,\,\langle m^{\rm tot}_{Q}\rangle_{L}\approx-\langle\Phi\rangle_{L}\,.\,

(26.0.5)

From (
26.0.5
) the fion masses are
\mu^{\rm pole}_{1}(\Phi)\sim\mu_{\Phi}\gg\Lambda_{Q}

On the whole, the mass spectrum looks in these
(N_{F}-2N_{c})
L - vacua as
follows:

a) there is a large number of hadrons made of weakly coupled and weakly confined
non-relativistic quarks with masses

SYM is

\sqrt{\sigma}\sim\langle{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(L)}\rangle\ll m^{\rm pole}_{Q,\,L})

\sim\langle{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(L)}\rangle\sim\Lambda_{Q}(\mu_{\Phi}/\Lambda_{Q})^{N_{F}/3(N_{F}-2N_{c})}\ll m^{\rm pole}_{Q,\,L}

c) the lightest are
N^{2}_{F}
fions with the masses
\mu^{\rm pole}(\Phi)\sim\mu_{\Phi}\ll\langle{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(L)}\rangle
.

The overall hierarchies look as

\displaystyle\Lambda_{Q}\ll\mu_{1}^{\rm pole}(\Phi)\sim\mu_{\Phi}\ll\langle{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(L)}\rangle\ll m^{\rm\,pole}_{Q,\,L}\sim\Lambda_{Q}\Bigl(\frac{\mu_{\Phi}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\frac{N_{c}}{N_{F}-2N_{c}}}.

(26.0.6)
