Dual theory. Unbroken flavor symmetry.

\hskip 28.45274pt\mathbf{2N_{c}<N_{F}<3N_{c}\,,\,\mu_{\Phi}\gg\Lambda_{Q}}

The dual theory in the UV region
\mu>\Lambda_{Q}
and at
2N_{c}<N_{F}<3N_{c}

is taken as UV free. The largest mass in the case considered is that of dual
quarks (neglecting here and below in this section all logarithmic factors due
to ], the RG-evolution)

\displaystyle\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,\,L}\sim\frac{\langle M\rangle_{L}=\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)\rangle_{L}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\sim\Lambda_{Q}\Biggl(\frac{\mu_{\Phi}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Biggr)^{\frac{{\overline{N}}_{c}}{N_{F}-2N_{c}}}\gg\Lambda_{Q},

(27.0.1)

while the gluon masses due to possible higgsing of dual quarks are smaller

{\overline{\mu}}^{\,2}_{\rm{gl,\,L}}\sim\langle{\overline{q}}q\rangle_{L}=\langle m_{Q}^{\rm tot}\rangle\Lambda_{Q}\sim\langle\Phi\rangle\Lambda_{Q}\sim\frac{\Lambda_{Q}\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)\rangle_{L}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\sim\Lambda_{Q}^{2}\Biggl(\frac{\mu_{\Phi}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Biggr)^{\frac{N_{c}}{N_{F}-2N_{c}}}\,,

\displaystyle\frac{{\overline{\mu}}_{\rm gl}}{\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,\,L}}\sim\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\Biggr)^{\frac{2N_{F}-3N_{c}}{2(N_{F}-2N_{c})}}\ll 1\,.

(27.0.2)

Therefore, the dual quarks are in the Hq (heavy quark) phase and are not
higgsed   but confined. This is self-consistent, because otherwise the global
flavor symmetry
SU(N_{F}>{\overline{N}}_{c})
will be broken spontaneously due to the rank
restriction. After they are integrated out at scales
\mu<\mu^{\rm pole}_{q}

in the weak
coupling regime
, there remain
N_{F}^{2}
fions
\Phi
,
N_{F}^{2}
mions
M

and the
SU({\overline{N}}_{c})
SYM with the scale factor of its gauge coupling

\displaystyle{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(L)}=\Bigl(\Lambda_{Q}^{\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}}\det\frac{M}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{1/3{\overline{N}}_{c}},\,\,\frac{\langle{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(L)}\rangle}{\Lambda_{Q}}\sim\Bigl(\frac{\mu_{\Phi}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\frac{N_{F}}{3(N_{F}-2N_{c})}}\gg\frac{\mu_{\Phi}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\gg 1,\,\,\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}=3{\overline{N}}_{c}-N_{F}>0\,.

(27.0.3)

After integrating out all dual gluons at scales
\mu<\langle{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(L)}\rangle

through the Veneziano-Yankielowics (VY) procedure
[
15
]
, the Lagrangian
looks as, see (
24.0.1
) for
{\cal W}_{\Phi}
,

\displaystyle K\sim{\rm Tr}\,\Bigl(\frac{M^{\dagger}M}{\Lambda_{Q}^{2}}+\Phi^{\dagger}\Phi\,\Bigr)\,,\hskip 11.74988pt{\cal W}=-{\overline{N}}_{c}\Bigl(\Lambda_{Q}^{\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}}\det\frac{M}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{1/{\overline{N}}_{c}}+{\rm Tr}\,(m_{Q}-\Phi)M+{\cal W}_{\Phi}\,.

(27.0.4)

All fions
\Phi
have masses
\mu^{\rm pole}(\Phi)\sim\mu_{\Phi}
and can be
integrated out at scales
\mu<\mu_{\Phi}
, resulting in the lower energy Lagrangian
of   mions

\displaystyle K_{M}\sim{\rm Tr}\,\frac{M^{\dagger}M}{\Lambda_{Q}^{2}}\,,\,\,\hskip 11.74988pt{\cal W}=-{\overline{N}}_{c}\Bigl(\frac{\det M}{\Lambda_{Q}^{\rm{b_{o}}}}\Bigr)^{1/{\overline{N}}_{c}}+{\cal W}_{M}\,,

(27.0.5)

{\cal W}_{M}=m_{Q}{\rm Tr}\,M-\frac{1}{2\mu_{\Phi}}\Biggl[{\rm Tr}\,(M^{2})-\frac{1}{N_{c}}({\rm Tr}\,M)^{2}\Biggr].

From (
27.0.5
) the mion masses are

\displaystyle\mu^{\rm pole}(M)\sim\frac{\Lambda_{Q}^{2}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\ll\Lambda_{Q}\,.

(27.0.6)

On the whole, the mass spectrum looks in these
(N_{F}-2N_{c})
dual L - vacua as
follows:

a) there is a large number of hadrons made of the weakly coupled and weakly
confined non-relativistic dual quarks with masses
\mu^{\rm pole}_{q}\sim\Lambda_{Q}(\mu_{\Phi}/\Lambda_{Q})^{{\overline{N}}_{c}/(N_{F}-2N_{c})}
(the tension of the confining string
originating from the
SU({\overline{N}}_{c})
dual SYM is
\sqrt{\sigma}\sim\langle{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(L)}\rangle\ll\mu^{\rm pole}_{q})
;

b) a large number of gluonia with the mass scale
\sim\langle{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(L)}\rangle\sim\Lambda_{Q}(\mu_{\Phi}/\Lambda_{Q})^{N_{F}/3(N_{F}-2N_{c})}
;

c) there are
N_{F}^{2}
fions with masses
\mu^{\rm pole}(\Phi)\sim\mu_{\Phi}
;

d) the lightest are
N^{2}_{F}
mions with masses
\mu^{\rm pole}(M)\sim(\Lambda_{Q}^{2}/\mu_{\Phi})\ll\Lambda_{Q}
.

The overall hierarchies look here as:

\mu^{\rm pole}(M)\ll\Lambda_{Q}\ll\mu^{\rm pole}(\Phi)\sim\mu_{\Phi}\ll\langle{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(L)}\rangle\ll\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,\,L}\sim\Lambda_{Q}\Biggl(\frac{\mu_{\Phi}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Biggr)^{\frac{{\overline{N}}_{c}}{N_{F}-2N_{c}}}\,.

Comparing the mass spectra in these L - vacua of the direct theory in section
26   and the dual one in this section it is seen that they are parametrically
different.

We would like to emphasize also that quarks in both direct and dual theories
are   simultaneously weakly coupled in these L - vacua, so that their dynamics is
clear and
we need not any additional dynamical assumptions to calculate
the   mass spectra
.

28

Direct theory. Broken flavor symmetry.

\hskip 28.45274pt\mathbf{2N_{c}<N_{F}<3N_{c}\,,\,\mu_{\Phi}\gg\Lambda_{Q}}

28.1

Lt - vacua

The only qualitative difference with the L - vacua in section 26
is that the flavor symmetry is broken spontaneously in these Lt - vacua,

\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle\neq\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle
, see (
25.2.5
), and
fions
\Phi_{1}^{2}
and
\Phi_{2}^{1}
are the Nambu-Goldstone
particles here and are exactly massless.

28.2

br1 - vacua

In these vacua with
n_{1}<N_{c}
and
m_{1}\ll\Lambda_{Q}\ll\mu_{\Phi}\ll\Lambda_{Q}^{2}/m_{Q}
,   the regime is conformal at scales
m^{\rm pole}_{Q,2}\ll\mu\ll\Lambda_{Q}

(see below)
and potentially most important masses look here as follows
(
z_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu<\Lambda_{Q})\ll 1
is the quark renormalization factor in the conformal
regime).

The gluon masses due to possible higgsing of quarks are, see (
25.2.4
)

\displaystyle\hskip-8.53581pt\Bigl(\mu^{\rm pole}_{\rm gl,1}\Bigr)^{2}\sim z_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu^{\rm pole}_{\rm gl,1})\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{\rm br1}\sim\Lambda_{Q}^{2}\Bigl(\frac{m_{Q}\mu_{\Phi}}{\Lambda_{Q}^{2}}\Bigr)^{\frac{N_{F}}{3{\overline{N}}_{c}}}\gg\mu^{2}_{\rm gl,2},\,\,z_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu^{\rm pole}_{\rm gl,1})\sim\Bigl(\frac{\mu^{\rm pole}_{\rm gl,1}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\frac{3N_{c}-N_{F}}{N_{F}}}\ll 1,\,\,\,

(28.2.1)

while the quark masses are

\displaystyle\hskip 11.74988pt\langle m^{\rm tot}_{Q,2}\rangle=\frac{\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{\rm br1}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\sim m_{Q}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt{\tilde{m}}^{\rm pole}_{Q,2}\sim\frac{\langle m^{\rm tot}_{Q,2}\rangle}{z_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q},{\tilde{m}}^{\rm pole}_{Q,2})}\sim\Lambda_{Q}\Bigl(\frac{m_{Q}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{N_{F}/3N_{c}}\gg m^{\rm pole}_{Q,1}\,,

(28.2.2)

\displaystyle\frac{{\tilde{m}}^{\rm pole}_{Q,2}}{\mu^{\rm pole}_{\rm gl,1}}\sim\Bigl(\frac{m_{1}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\Bigr)^{N_{F}/3{\overline{N}}_{c}}\ll 1\,.

(28.2.3)

Therefore, the quarks
Q^{1},{\overline{Q}}_{1}
are higgsed and the overall phase is

Higgs_{1}-HQ_{2}
. If we take
2n_{1}<\rm{b_{o}}=(3N_{c}-N_{F})
, then
{\rm b}^{\prime}_{\rm o}=(\rm{b_{o}}-2n_{1})>0
and the lower
energy theory with
SU(N_{c}-n_{1})
colors and
n_{2}
flavors will be in the
conformal regime at scales
m^{\rm pole}_{Q,2}\sim\langle{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(\rm br1)}\rangle\ll\mu\ll\mu^{\rm pole}_{\rm gl,1}
. Then all results for the mass
spectra will be the same as in section 10.1 of
[
17
]
(and only the ranges
of
N_{F}
are different in
[
17
]
and here).

29

Dual theory. Broken flavor symmetry.

\hskip 28.45274pt\mathbf{2N_{c}<N_{F}<3N_{c}\,,\,\mu_{\Phi}\gg\Lambda_{Q}}

29.1

Lt - vacua

The main difference with the L - vacua in section 27 is that the
flavor symmetry is broken spontaneously in these dual Lt - vacua,
\langle M_{1}\rangle\neq\langle M_{2}\rangle
. The fions have masses
\mu^{\rm pole}(\Phi)\sim\mu_{\Phi}\gg\Lambda_{Q}
and are dynamically irrelevant at scales
\mu<\mu_{\Phi}
.
The low energy Lagrangian of mions is (
27.0.5
), but the mion masses
look ], now as

\displaystyle\mu^{\rm pole}(M_{1}^{1})\sim\mu^{\rm pole}(M_{2}^{2})\sim\frac{\Lambda_{Q}^{2}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\ll\Lambda_{Q},\hskip 11.74988pt\mu^{\rm pole}(M_{1}^{2})=\mu^{\rm pole}(M_{2}^{1})=0\,.

(29.1.1)

Clearly, the parametric differences remain in mass spectra of the direct and
dual theories in these Lt-vacua.

29.2

br1 - vacua

Not going into any details we give here the results only. The
overall phase is
Hq_{1}-Hq_{2}
(i.e. heavy confined quarks). The regime at

\mu<\Lambda_{Q}
is conformal, and the massless Nambu-Goldstone particles here are the
mions
M_{1}^{2}
and
M_{2}^{1}
. The mass spectra are as in section 11.1 of

[
17
]
, the only difference is that
Z_{q}\rightarrow 1
here because

\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}=(3{\overline{N}}_{c}-N_{F})/N_{F}=O(1)
now (and only the ranges of
N_{F}
are different in

[
17
]
and here).

More details can be found in the original paper
[
25
]
.

.

Part IIc.
\mathbf{N_{c}<N_{F}<3N_{c}/2}

30

Introduction

This part continues studies of
{\cal N}=1
SQCD-type theories (see
above). Aso considered here are the
SU(N_{c})
SQCD-type (direct)
theory (and its Seiberg’s dual variant with
SU({\overline{N}}_{c}=N_{F}-N_{c})
dual
colors), with
N_{F}
flavors of quarks
{\overline{Q}}_{j},Q^{i}
with the
small mass parameter
m_{Q}\ll\Lambda_{Q}
in the Lagrandian.
Besides, there are
N_{F}^{2}
additional colorless
but flavored fields
\Phi^{j}_{i}
with the large mass parameter

\mu_{\Phi}\gg\Lambda_{Q}
. But now considered in details is
the region

N_{c}<N_{F}<3N_{c}/2
where the UV free direct
SU(N_{c})
theory
is very strongly coupled at scales
\mu<\Lambda_{Q}
, with the parametrically
large gauge coupling
a(\mu\ll\Lambda_{Q})\gg 1
.
The mass spectra of this direct theory in various vacua and at
different values of
\mu_{\Phi}
are calculated in this case within the
dynamical scenario introduced by the author in
[
20
]
. This
scenario assumes that massive quarks with
m_{Q}\neq 0
in such

{\cal N}=1
SQCD-type theories without elementary colored adjoint
scalars can be in two
standard
phases only. These are either the
HQ (heavy quark) phase where they are confined or the Higgs phase
where they are condenced in the vaxuum state.

Similarly to previous studies of this theory within the conformal
window
3N_{c}/2<N_{F}<3N_{c}
, it is shown here that, in the direct

SU(N_{c})
theory, due to the powerlike RG evolution, the seemingly
heavy and dynamically irrelevant at scales
\mu<\Lambda_{Q}
fields
\Phi^{j}_{i}

can become light and relevant at lower energies, and there appear then
two additional generation of light
\Phi
-particles with masses

\mu_{2,3}^{\rm pole}(\Phi)\ll\Lambda_{Q}
.

The calculated mass spectra of this strongly coupled at
\mu<\Lambda_{Q}

direct
SU(N_{c})
theory were compared to those of its weakly coupled
at
\mu<\Lambda_{Q}
Seiberg’s dual
SU(N_{F}-N_{c})
variant and appeared to be

parametrically different
.

All results obtained within the used dynamical scenario from

[
20
]
look self-consistent. In other words, no internal
contradictions were encountered in all cases considered. It is worth
also to remind that
this dynamical scenario satisfies all those tests
which were used as checks of
the Seiberg hypothesis about the equivalence of the direct and dual
theories. The parametrical differences of mass spectra of the direct
and dual theories show, in particular, that all these tests, although
necessary, may well be insufficient
. See also
[
27
]
).

Recall that the Lagrangian of this (direct)
\Phi
-theory at the scale

\mu=\Lambda_{Q}
has the form

41
41

41

The gluon exponents are always implied in the Kahler terms. Besides,
here and everywhere below in the text we neglect for simplicity all
RG-evolution effects if they are logarithmic only.

K={\rm Tr}\,(\Phi^{\dagger}\Phi)+{\rm Tr}\Bigl(\,Q^{\dagger}Q+Q\rightarrow{\overline{Q}}\,\Bigr)\,,\hskip 11.74988pt{\cal W}=\frac{2\pi}{\alpha(\mu=\Lambda_{Q})}S+{\cal W}_{\rm matter}\,,

\displaystyle{\cal W}_{\rm matter}={\cal W}_{\Phi}+{\cal W}_{Q},\,{\cal W}_{\Phi}=\frac{\mu_{\Phi}}{2}\Biggl[{\rm Tr}\,(\Phi^{2})-\frac{1}{{\overline{N}}_{c}}\Bigl({\rm Tr}\,\Phi\Bigr)^{2}\Bigg],\,{\cal W}_{Q}={\rm Tr}\,\Bigl({\overline{Q}}(m_{Q}-\Phi)Q\Bigr).

(30.0.1)

Here :
\mu_{\Phi}\gg\Lambda_{Q}
and
m_{Q}\ll\Lambda_{Q}
are mass parameters, the
traces in (
30.0.1
) are over color and/or flavor indices,

S=\sum_{A,\gamma}W^{A}_{\gamma}W^{A,\,\gamma}/32\pi^{2}
, where

W^{A}_{\gamma}
is the gauge field strength,
A=1...N_{c}^{2}-1,\,\gamma=1,2
,
a(\mu)=N_{c}\alpha(\mu)/2\pi=N_{c}g^{2}(\mu)/8\pi^{2}
is the
running gauge coupling with its scale factor
\Lambda_{Q}
,
\,Q^{i}_{\beta},{\overline{Q}}_{j}^{\,\beta},\,\,\beta=1...N_{c},\,\,i,j=1...N_{F}
are the quark fields. This
normalization of fields is used everywhere below in the text. Besides,
the perturbatively exact NSVZ
\beta_{NSVZ}
functions for (effectively)
massless SUSY theories
[
12
,
13
]
are used in this paper.

All dynamical properties of theory (
30.0.1
) : the RG evolution,
phase states, mass spectra etc., depends essentially on the value of

N_{F}/N_{c}
. For instance, it enters at
\mu<\Lambda_{Q}
: a) the weakly
coupled IR free logarithmic regime with the gauge coupling

a(\mu\ll\Lambda_{Q})\sim 1/\log(\Lambda_{Q}/\mu)\ll 1
at
N_{F}>3N_{c}
;  b) the
strongly coupled conformal regime with
a(\mu\ll\Lambda_{Q})=a_{*}={\rm const}=O(1)
(in general) at
3N_{c}/2<N_{F}<3N_{c}
;  c) the (very) strongly
coupled regime with
a(\mu\ll\Lambda_{Q})\sim(\Lambda_{Q}/\mu)^{\nu_{Q}\,>\,0}\gg 1,\,\nu=(3N_{c}-2N_{F})/(N_{F}-N_{c})
at
N_{c}<N_{F}<3N_{c}/2
. Besides, the mass
spectra at given
N_{F}/N_{c}
depend essentially on the considered
vacuum.

In parallel with the direct
\Phi
-theory (
30.0.1
), we study also
proposed by Seiberg’s
[
4
,
5
,
2
]
its dual variant, the

d\Phi
-theory with
SU({\overline{N}}_{c}=N_{F}-N_{c})
dual colors,
N_{F}
flavors of
dual quarks
q_{i}^{\beta},{\overline{q}}^{\,j}_{\beta},\,\,\beta=1...{\overline{N}}_{c}
,
and with
N_{F}^{2}
additional colorless but flavored elementary fields

M^{i}_{j}\rightarrow({\overline{Q}}_{j}Q^{i})
. Its Lagrangian at
\mu=\Lambda_{Q}
looks as,
see (
30.0.1
) for
{\cal W}_{\Phi}
,

{\overline{K}}={\rm Tr}\,(\Phi^{\dagger}\Phi)+{\rm Tr}\,\Bigl(\frac{M^{\dagger}M}{\Lambda_{Q}^{2}}\Bigr)+{\rm Tr}\,\Bigl(q^{\dagger}q+(q\rightarrow{\overline{q}})\Bigr)\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\overline{\cal W}=\,\,\frac{2\pi}{{\overline{\alpha}}(\mu=\Lambda_{Q})}\,{\overline{S}}+{\overline{\cal W}}_{\rm matter}\,,

\displaystyle{\overline{\cal W}}_{\rm matter}={\cal W}_{\Phi}+{\cal W}_{\Phi M}+{\cal W}_{q},\,{\cal W}_{\Phi M}={\rm Tr}\,(m^{\rm tot}_{Q}M)={\rm Tr}\Bigl((m_{Q}-\Phi)M\Bigr),\,{\cal W}_{q}=-\rm{Tr}\Bigl({\overline{q}}\frac{M}{\Lambda_{Q}}q\Bigr).

(30.0.2)

Here :  the number of dual colors is
{\overline{N}}_{c}=(N_{F}-N_{c})
,

M^{i}_{j}
are
N_{F}^{2}
Seiberg’s elementary mion fields,
M^{i}_{j}\rightarrow({\overline{Q}}_{j}Q^{i})
,
{\overline{a}}(\mu)={\overline{N}}_{c}{\overline{\alpha}}(\mu)/2\pi={\overline{N}}_{c}{\overline{g}}^{2}(\mu)/8\pi^{2}
is the dual running gauge coupling (with its scale
parameter
\Lambda_{q}=-\Lambda_{Q}
),
{\overline{S}}=\sum_{B,\beta}{\rm\overline{W}}^{\,B}_{\beta}\,{\rm\overline{W}}^{\,B,\,\beta}/32\pi^{2}
,
{\rm\overline{W}}^{\,B}_{\beta}
is the dual gluon field strength,
B=1...{\overline{N}}_{c}^{2}-1,\,\,\beta=1,\,2
.

The gluino condensates of the direct and dual theories are matched,

\langle{-\,\overline{S}}\rangle=\langle S\rangle={\Lambda}_{SYM}^{3}
, as well as

\langle M^{i}_{j}\rangle\equiv\langle M^{i}_{j}(\mu=\Lambda_{Q})\rangle=\langle{\overline{Q}}_{j}Q^{i}(\mu=\Lambda_{Q})\rangle\equiv\langle{\overline{Q}}_{j}Q^{i}\rangle
, and the
scale parameter
\Lambda_{q}
of the dual gauge coupling is taken as

(-\Lambda_{q})=\Lambda_{Q}
. At
N_{c}<N_{F}<3N_{c}/2
this dual theory is IR free and
logarithmically weakly coupled at
\mu<\Lambda_{Q},\,\,{\overline{a}}(\mu\ll\Lambda_{Q})\sim 1/{\log(\Lambda_{Q}/\mu)\ll 1}
.

We studied these theories (
30.0.1
) and (
30.0.2
) in

[
17
,
25
]
at values of
N_{F}
in the range
3N_{c}/2<N_{F}<3N_{c}
,
i.e. within the conformal window. The purpose of article
[
26
]

was to consider in details the range
N_{c}<N_{F}<3N_{c}/2
.

According to Seiberg’s view of the standard direct (i.e. without
fields
\Phi^{i}_{j}
)
{\cal N}=1
SQCD at

N_{c}+1<N_{F}<3N_{c}/2
, with the scale factor
\Lambda_{Q}
of
SU(N_{c})
gauge
coupling and direct quarks with
m_{Q}=0
(or with
0<m_{Q}\ll\Lambda
),
the regime of the direct theory at
\mu<\Lambda_{Q}
is in this case:
‘confinement without chiral symmetry breaking’
(as far as small

m_{Q}\neq 0
can be neglected). And
the dual theory is considered
as the lower energy form of the direct theory
. This
means that all
direct quarks remained massless (or light), but hadrons made from
these quarks and direct gluons
acquired large
masses
\sim\Lambda_{Q}
due to mysterious confinement with the string
tension

\sigma^{1/2}\sim\Lambda_{Q}
, and decoupled at
\mu<\Lambda_{Q}
. Instead of
them, there mysteriously appeared massless (or light) composite solitons.
These last are particles of the dual theory.

This picture was questioned in
[
11
]
(see section 7 therein). It
was argued that, with the unbroken at
m_{Q}\rightarrow 0
chiral flavor symmetry

SU(N_{F})_{L}\times SU(N_{F})_{R}
and unbroken R-charge, it is impossible to
write at
\mu\sim\Lambda_{Q}
the nonsingular superpotential of the effective
Lagrangian of massive flavored hadrons with masses
\sim\Lambda_{Q}
made from
direct massless (or light) quarks.

42
42

42

This is similar to our ordinary QCD with the scale factor
\Lambda
of
the
SU(3)
gauge coupling. Then, with confinement and with three
massless (or light) quarks, but without breaking of chiral symmetry

SU(3)_{L}\times SU(3)_{R}
, it is impossible e.g. to have in the
effective hadron Lagrangian at
\mu\sim\Lambda
the massive nucleons
with the mass
\sim\Lambda
, as the term
\sim\Lambda{\overline{N}}N
in
the potential is incompatible with the unbroken chiral symmetry. And
the situation in
{\cal N}=1
SQCD is even more restrictive because
the superpotential is holomorphic and due to additional R-charge
conservation.

We also recall here the following. There is no confinement in
Yukawa-type theories without gauge interactions. The confinement
originates
only
from the (part of) YM or
{\cal N}=1
SYM in

{\cal N}=1
SQCD-type theories unbroken by (possibly) higgsed quarks.
And because
{\cal N}=1
SYM has only one dimensional parameter

\langle{\Lambda}_{SYM}\rangle=\langle S\rangle^{1/3}
, the string tension is

\sigma^{1/2}\sim\langle{\Lambda}_{SYM}\rangle
. But in the standard
{\cal N}=1

SQCD the value of
{\Lambda}_{SYM}
is well known:
{\Lambda}_{SYM}=(\Lambda_{Q}^{\rm{b_{o}}}\det{m_{Q}})^{1/3N_{c}}\ll\Lambda_{Q}
at
m_{Q}\ll\Lambda_{Q}
Therefore, such
SYM cannot produce confinement with
the string tension
\sim\Lambda_{Q}
, but only with

\sigma^{1/2}\sim{\Lambda}_{SYM}\ll\Lambda_{Q}
(and there is no
confinement at all at
m_{Q}\rightarrow 0
).

43
43

43

And the same for the direct SQCD-type
\Phi
-theory considered here:

\langle{\Lambda}_{SYM}\rangle=(\Lambda_{Q}^{\rm{b_{o}}}\det\langle m^{\rm tot}_{Q}\rangle)^{1/3N_{c}}\ll\Lambda_{Q}
. Therefore, such SYM cannot produce
confinement with
\sigma^{1/2}\sim\Lambda_{Q}
, only with

\sigma^{1/2}\sim\langle{\Lambda}_{SYM}\rangle\ll\Lambda_{Q}
.

For these reasons, as was argued in detail in section 7 of
[
11
]

and in Conclusions of
[
27
]
, the UV free direct theory
(
30.0.1
) with
N_{c}<N_{F}<3N_{c}/2
flavors of light quarks enters

smoothly
at
\mu<\Lambda_{Q}
into the effectively massless perturbative
(very) strongly coupled regime with
a(\mu\ll\Lambda_{Q})\sim(\Lambda_{Q}/\mu)^{\nu_{Q}\,>\,0}\gg 1
(and NSVZ
\beta
-function allows this).
This is qualitatively similar to situation in the conformal window at

3N_{c}/2<N_{F}<3N_{c}
, where the direct theory enters smoothly at

\mu<\Lambda_{Q}
into the conformal regime with the coupling

a(\mu\ll\Lambda_{Q})={\rm const}\sim 1
, and with
all its light quarks and
gluons remaining effectively massless
.

For a description of the RG evolution and calculations of mass spectra
in various vacua in this strong coupling regime we use the dynamical
scenario introduced in
[
20
]
. This scenario assumes that quarks
in such
{\cal N}=1
SQCD-type theories can be in two
standard

phases only. These are either the HQ (heavy quark) phase where

\langle{\overline{Q}}\rangle=\langle Q\rangle=0
and they are confined, or
the Higgs phase where they form nonzero coherent condensate

\langle{\overline{Q}}\rangle=\langle Q\rangle\neq 0
breaking the color
symmetry. The word
standard
implies here also that, unlike e.g.
very special
{\cal N}=2
SQCD with enhanced supersymmetry, in such

{\cal N}=1
theories without elementary colored adjoint scalars, no

additional

44
44

44

i.e. in addition to the massless particles due to
spontaneously broken global flavor symmetry

parametrically light solitons (e.g. magnetic monopoles or dyons) are
formed at those scales where quarks decouple as heavy or are higgsed.

Within this scenario, were calculated the mass spectra of the strongly
coupled direct theory (
30.0.1
) in different vacua. It was shown in

[
26
]
(as well as in
[
17
,
25
]
) that the use of
this scenario leads to the results for the mass spectra which do not
contradict to any proven results and look self-consistent, i.e. no
internal inconsistences are incountered in all cases considered.

Similarly to our previous studies of this theory (
30.0.1
) within
the conformal window at
3N_{c}/2<N_{F}<3N_{c}
in
[
25
,
17
]
, it was
shown in
[
26
]
that, due to a strong power-like RG evolution at scales

\mu<\Lambda_{Q}
in the direct theory, the seemingly heavy and dynamically
irrelevant fields
\Phi^{j}_{i}
with
\mu_{\Phi}\gg\Lambda_{Q}
can become light and
there appear then two additional generations of light
\Phi
-particles
with
\mu_{2,3}^{\rm pole}(\Phi)\ll\Lambda_{Q}
.

In parallel, we calculate the mass spectra in the Seiberg’s dual
theory (
30.0.2
). But, as explained above, this ”dual”  theory
has to be considered
not
as the literal or equivalent low energy
form of the direct
theory at scales
\mu<\Lambda_{Q}
, but simply as
a definite independent
theory
. This IR free theory with
N_{c}<N_{F}<3N_{c}/2
is logarithmically
weakly coupled at
\mu<\Lambda_{Q}
and so needs no additional dynamical
assumptions for calculations of its mass spectra. The mass spectra of
both direct and dual theories are then calculated and compared.

As described below in the text, the comparison of mass spectra of
direct and dual theories (
30.0.1
) and (
30.0.2
) shows,
similarly to the standard direct and dual
{\cal N}=1
SQCD theories
(i.e. those in (
30.0.1
) and (
30.0.2
) but without fields

\Phi^{j}_{i}
, see
[
20
]
), that these two mass spectra are
parametrically different
. It is worth to recall that the dynamical
scenario from
[
20
]
used in this article satisfies all those
tests which were used as checks of the Seiberg hypothesis about the
equivalence of the direct and dual theories. This shows, in
particular, that all these tests, although necessary, may well be
insufficient.

The paper is organized as follows. Because the global flavor symmetry

U(N_{F})
is unbroken or broken spontaneously as
U(N_{F})\rightarrow U({\rm n}_{1})\times U({\rm n}_{2})
in different vacua of
(
30.0.1
),(
30.0.2
), we consider these cases separately.
Besides, because the parametric behavior of quark condensates
and the whole dynamics are quite different in two regions

\mu_{\Phi}\gtrless m_{1}=\Lambda_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q}/m_{Q})^{(2N_{c}-N_{F})/N_{c}}
,
we also consider these regions separately. See contents.

The region
\mathbf{\Lambda_{Q}\ll\mu_{\Phi}\ll m_{1}=\Lambda_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q}/m_{Q})^{(2N_{c}-N_{F})/N_{c}}}

31

Unbroken flavor symmetry,   L-vacua

31.1

Direct theory

The quark condensates (here and everywhere below always at the scale

\mu=\Lambda_{Q}
) in these L - vacua with the multiplicity
(2N_{c}-N_{F})

look as, see section 16
(
\,\langle S\rangle
is the gluino condensate
summed over all its colors,
\rm{b_{o}}=3N_{c}-N_{F}
)

\displaystyle\langle{\overline{Q}}Q(\mu=\Lambda_{Q})\rangle_{L}\sim\Lambda_{Q}^{2}\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\Bigr)^{\frac{N_{F}-N_{c}}{2N_{c}-N_{F}}}\ll\Lambda_{Q}^{2},\,\,\langle S\rangle_{L}=\Bigl(\frac{\langle\det{\overline{Q}}Q\rangle_{L}}{\Lambda_{Q}^{\rm\rm{b_{o}}}}\Bigr)^{\frac{1}{N_{F}-N_{c}}}\sim\Lambda_{Q}^{3}\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\Bigr)_{,}^{\frac{N_{F}}{2N_{c}-N_{F}}}\,\,

(31.1.1)

while from the Konishi anomaly
[
14
]

\displaystyle\langle m_{Q}^{\rm tot}\rangle_{L}\equiv\langle m_{Q}-\Phi\rangle_{L}=\frac{\langle S\rangle_{L}}{({\overline{Q}}Q)_{L}}\sim\Lambda_{Q}\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\Bigr)^{\frac{N_{c}}{2N_{c}-N_{F}}}\ll\Lambda_{Q}\,.

(31.1.2)

As was argued in detail in section 7 of
[
11
]
and in Conclusions
of
[
27
]
, this UV free direct
\Phi
-theory with the gauge
coupling
a(\mu\gg\Lambda_{Q})\equiv N_{c}g^{2}(\mu\gg\Lambda_{Q})/8\pi^{2}\ll 1
enters
smoothly the strong coupling regime at the scale
\mu<\Lambda_{Q}
, with the
gauge coupling
a(\mu\ll\Lambda_{Q})\gg 1
. The values of anomalous
dimensions
\gamma_{Q},\,\gamma_{\Phi}
in the direct theory and

\gamma_{q}\,,\,\gamma_{M}
in the dual one at
N_{c}<N_{F}<3N_{c}/2

and
\mu\ll\Lambda_{Q}
are, see
[
11
,
26
]
and Appendix 1,

\gamma_{Q}=\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{N_{F}-N_{c}}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\gamma_{\Phi}=-2\gamma_{Q}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\gamma_{q}\rightarrow 0\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\gamma_{M}\rightarrow 0\,.

The potentially important masses look then as follows :

a) the quark pole mass

\displaystyle m^{\rm pole}_{Q,L}=\frac{\langle m_{Q}^{\rm tot}\rangle_{L}}{z_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q},m^{\rm pole}_{Q,L})}\sim\Lambda_{Q}\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\Bigr)^{\frac{N_{F}-N_{c}}{2N_{c}-N_{F}}},\,z_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q},m^{\rm pole}_{Q,L})=\Bigl(\frac{m^{\rm pole}_{Q,L}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\gamma_{Q}},\,\gamma_{Q}=\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{N_{F}-N_{c}}>1;\,\,\,\,\,\,

(31.1.3)

b) the gluon mass due to possible higgsing of quarks

\displaystyle\Bigl(\mu^{\rm pole}_{{\rm gl},L}\Bigr)^{2}\sim a(\mu^{\rm pole}_{{\rm gl},L})\,z_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu^{\rm pole}_{{\rm gl},L})({\overline{Q}}Q)_{L}\,,\hskip 11.74988ptz_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu^{\rm pole}_{{\rm gl},L})=\Bigl(\frac{\mu^{\rm pole}_{{\rm gl},L}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\gamma_{Q}}\ll 1,\,\,

(31.1.4)

\frac{d\,a(\mu\ll\Lambda_{Q})}{d\log\mu}=\beta_{NSVZ}(a)=\frac{a^{2}(\mu)}{a(\mu)-1}\,\frac{\rm{b_{o}}-N_{F}\gamma_{Q}}{N_{c}}\hskip 11.74988pt\xrightarrow{a(\mu)\gg 1}\hskip 11.74988pt-\,\nu_{Q}\,a(\mu),\hskip 11.74988pt\rm{b_{o}}=3N_{c}-N_{F}\,,

\nu_{Q}=\frac{N_{F}\gamma_{Q}-\rm{b_{o}}}{N_{c}}=\frac{3N_{c}-2N_{F}}{N_{F}-N_{c}}=\gamma_{Q}-1>0\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\,a(\mu=\mu^{\rm\,pole}_{{\rm gl},L}\ll\Lambda_{Q})\sim\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{\mu^{\rm pole}_{{\rm\,gl},L}}\Bigr)^{\nu_{Q}}\gg 1,

a(\mu^{\rm pole}_{{\rm gl},L})\,z_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu^{\rm pole}_{{\rm gl},L})\sim\frac{\mu^{\rm pole}_{{\rm gl},L}}{\Lambda_{Q}},\hskip 11.74988pt\mu^{\rm pole}_{{\rm gl},L}\sim\frac{({\overline{Q}}Q)_{L}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\sim\Lambda_{Q}\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\Bigr)^{\frac{N_{F}-N_{c}}{2N_{c}-N_{F}}}\sim m^{\rm pole}_{Q,L}\,.

Because the global non-Abelian flavor symmetry
SU(N_{F})
is unbroken
in these L-vacua, this means that
the quarks in such a case are
not higgsed due to the rank restriction

N_{c}<N_{F}
, as otherwise the
global flavor symmetry
SU(N_{F})
will be broken spontaneously.
Therefore,
the overall phase is in this case HQ
(heavy confined
quarks).

45
45

45

The same reasonings are used also everywhere below in similar cases.

The Lagrangian at scales
\mu
such that
\mu_{o}^{\rm str}<\mu<\Lambda_{Q}
looks as

\displaystyle K=z_{\Phi}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu)\,{\rm Tr}(\Phi^{\dagger}\Phi\,)+z_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu){\rm Tr}\,\Bigl(Q^{\dagger}Q+Q\rightarrow\bar{Q}\Bigr),\,z_{\Phi}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu)=1/z^{2}_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu),\,\,

(31.1.5)

z_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu\ll\Lambda_{Q})=\Bigl(\frac{\mu}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\gamma_{Q}}=\Bigl(\frac{\mu}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{N_{F}-N_{c}}}\ll 1,\,\,z_{\Phi}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu)=\Bigl(\frac{\mu}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\gamma_{\Phi}=-2\gamma_{Q}}=\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{\mu}\Bigr)^{\frac{2(2N_{c}-N_{F})}{N_{F}-N_{c}}}\gg 1.

\displaystyle{\mathcal{W}}={\cal W}_{\Phi}+{\rm Tr}\,(\,{\overline{Q}}\,m_{Q}^{\rm tot}Q\,)\,,\hskip 11.74988pt{\cal W}_{\Phi}=\frac{\mu_{\Phi}}{2}\Bigl({\rm Tr}\,(\Phi^{2})-\frac{1}{{\overline{N}}_{c}}({\rm Tr}\,\Phi)^{2}\Bigr),\hskip 11.74988ptm_{Q}^{\rm tot}=(m_{Q}-\Phi)\,.

(31.1.6)

Therefore, the running perturbative mass of
\Phi
is

\mu_{\Phi}(\mu\ll\Lambda_{Q})=\mu_{\Phi}/z_{\Phi}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu)\ll\mu_{\Phi}
and, if nothing
prevents, the field
\Phi
becomes dynamically relevant at scales

\mu<\mu_{o}^{\rm str},\,\mu_{\Phi}(\mu=\mu_{o}^{\rm str})=\mu_{o}^{\rm str}
,

\mu_{o}^{\rm str}=\Lambda_{Q}\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\Bigr)^{\frac{1}{2\gamma_{Q}-1}}=\Lambda_{Q}\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\Bigr)^{\frac{{\overline{N}}_{c}}{5N_{c}-3N_{F}}}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\gamma_{Q}=\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{{\overline{N}}_{c}}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt{\overline{N}}_{c}=N_{F}-N_{c}\,,

\displaystyle\frac{\mu_{o}^{\rm str}}{m^{\rm pole}_{Q,L}}\sim\Bigl(\frac{\mu_{\Phi}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\Delta}\gg 1\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\Delta=\frac{{\overline{N}}_{c}(3N_{c}-2N_{F})}{(2N_{c}-N_{F})(5N_{c}-3N_{F})}>0\,,

(31.1.7)

and there is the second generation of all
N_{F}^{2}
fions
\Phi^{j}_{i}

with
\mu_{2,L}^{\rm pole}(\Phi)\sim\mu_{o}^{\rm str}\gg m_{Q,L}^{\rm pole}
. Besides, even at lower scales

m^{\rm pole}_{Q,L}\ll\mu\ll\mu_{o}^{\rm str}
where the fields

\Phi^{j}_{i}
became already effectively massless (in the sense

\mu_{\Phi}(\mu)\ll\mu
) and so dynamically relevant, the quark
anomalous dimension
\gamma_{Q}=(2N_{c}-N_{F})/{\overline{N}}_{c}
remains the same, as well as

\gamma_{\Phi}=-2\gamma_{Q}
, see Appendix 1 .

At
\mu<m^{\rm pole}_{Q,L}
all quarks decouple as heavy ones and the
RG evolution of all fields
\Phi
becomes frozen, but this happens in
the region where they are already relevant, i.e. the running mass of
fions
\mu_{\Phi}(\mu)
is
\mu_{\Phi}(\mu=m^{\rm pole}_{Q,L})\ll m^{\rm pole}_{Q,L}
. This means that there is the third generation of
all
N_{F}^{2}
fions with
\mu^{\rm pole}_{3,L}(\Phi)\ll m^{\rm pole}_{Q,L}
, see below.

The lower energy theory at
\mu<m^{\rm pole}_{Q,L}
is
{\cal N}=1\,\,SU(N_{c})
SYM in the strong coupling regime (plus
N_{F}^{2}
colorless
fions
\Phi
). The scale factor
\langle{\Lambda}_{SYM}\rangle_{L}
of its gauge
coupling is determined from the matching, see
(
31.1.3
),(
31.1.8
) and section 7 in
[
11
]
:

\frac{d\,a^{\rm str}_{YM}(\mu)}{d\log\mu}=\beta^{YM}_{NSVZ}(a^{\rm str}_{YM})=\frac{3(a^{\rm str}_{YM})^{2}}{a^{\rm str}_{YM}-1}\,\,\,\,\xrightarrow{a^{\rm str}_{YM}\gg 1}\,\,\,3\,a^{\rm str}_{YM}(\mu)\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\,a^{\rm str}_{YM}(\mu\gg{\Lambda}_{SYM})\sim\Bigl(\frac{\mu}{{\Lambda}_{SYM}}\Bigr)^{3}\gg 1,

\displaystyle a_{+}(\mu=m^{\rm pole}_{Q,L}\ll\Lambda_{Q})\sim\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{m^{\rm pole}_{Q,L}}\Bigr)^{\nu_{Q}}=a^{\rm str}_{YM}(\mu=m^{\rm pole}_{Q,L}\gg{\Lambda}_{SYM})\sim\Bigl(\frac{m^{\rm pole}_{Q,L}}{\langle{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(L)}\rangle}\Bigr)^{3}\gg 1\,.

(31.1.8)

From (
31.1.3
),(
31.1.8
)

\displaystyle\langle{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(L)}\rangle^{3}\sim\Lambda_{Q}^{3}\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\Bigr)^{\frac{N_{F}}{2N_{c}-N_{F}}}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\frac{\langle{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(L)}\rangle}{m^{\rm pole}_{Q,L}}\sim\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\Bigr)^{\frac{3N_{c}-2N_{F}}{3(2N_{c}-N_{F})}}\ll 1\,,

(31.1.9)

as it should be because, see (
31.1.1
),

\langle{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(L)}\rangle^{3}\equiv\langle S\rangle_{L}=\Biggl(\frac{\det\langle{\overline{Q}}Q\rangle_{L}}{\Lambda_{Q}^{\rm\rm{b_{o}}}}\Biggr)^{1/{\overline{N}}_{c}}\sim\Lambda_{Q}^{3}\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\Bigr)^{\frac{N_{F}}{2N_{c}-N_{F}}}.

After lowering the scale down to
\mu<\langle{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(L)}\rangle
and integrating
out all gauge degrees of freedom via the VY (Veneziano-Yankielowicz)-procedure

[
15
]
, the low energy Lagrangian looks as, see
(
31.1.5
),(
31.1.6
),

\displaystyle K=z_{\Phi}(\Lambda_{Q},m^{\rm pole}_{Q,L})\,{\rm Tr}(\Phi^{\dagger}\Phi\,),\,z_{\Phi}(\Lambda_{Q},m^{\rm pole}_{Q,L})=\frac{1}{z^{2}_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q},m^{\rm pole}_{Q,L})}=\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{m^{\rm pole}_{Q,L}}\Bigr)^{\frac{2(2N_{c}-N_{F})}{N_{F}-N_{c}}}\gg 1,\,\,\,\,

(31.1.10)

{\cal W}={\cal W}_{\Phi}+{\cal W}_{\rm non-pert}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt{\cal W}_{\rm non-pert}=N_{c}\Bigl({\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(L)}\Bigr)^{3}=N_{c}\Bigl(\Lambda_{Q}^{\rm\rm{b_{o}}}\det m^{\rm tot}_{Q}\Bigr)^{1/N_{c}}\,.

From (
31.1.10
), the pole masses of
N_{F}^{2}
third generation fions

\Phi^{j}_{i}
are (the contributions to
\mu^{\rm pole}_{3,L}(\Phi)
from
{\cal W}_{\Phi}
and
{\cal W}_{\rm non-pert}
are parametrically the same)

\displaystyle\mu^{\rm pole}_{3,L}(\Phi)\sim\frac{\mu_{\Phi}}{z_{\Phi}(\Lambda_{Q},m^{\rm pole}_{Q,L})}\sim\frac{\Lambda_{Q}^{2}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\hskip 11.74988pt\frac{\mu^{\rm pole}_{3,L}(\Phi)}{\langle{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(L)}\rangle}\sim\Biggl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\Biggr)^{\frac{2(3N_{c}-2N_{F})}{3(2N_{F}-N_{c})}}\ll 1\,.

(31.1.11)

On the whole for the case considered.

1)   All quarks
{\overline{Q}}_{j},Q^{i}
are in the HQ (heavy quark) phase and weakly
confined (i.e. the tension of the confining string originating from
{\cal N}=1\,\,SU(N_{c})
SYM is much smaller than quark masses,

\sqrt{\sigma}\sim\langle{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(L)}\rangle\ll m^{\rm pole}_{Q,L}
, see
(
31.1.3
),(
31.1.9
).

2)   There is a large number of
SU(N_{c})
gluonia with the mass scale

\sim\langle{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(L)}\rangle=\langle S\rangle^{1/3}_{L}
, see
(
31.1.9
).

3) There are two generations of
N_{F}^{2}
fions
\Phi^{j}_{i}
with masses

\displaystyle\mu^{\rm pole}_{2,L}(\Phi)\sim\mu_{o}^{\rm str}\sim\Lambda_{Q}\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\Bigr)^{\frac{{\overline{N}}_{c}}{5N_{c}-3N_{F}}},\hskip 11.74988pt\mu^{\rm pole}_{3,L}(\Phi)\sim\frac{\Lambda_{Q}^{2}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\,.

(31.1.12)

The overall mass hierarchies look as

\displaystyle\mu^{\rm pole}_{3,L}(\Phi)\ll\langle{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(L)}\rangle\ll m^{\rm pole}_{Q,L}\ll\mu^{\rm pole}_{2,L}(\Phi)\ll\Lambda_{Q}\ll\mu^{\rm pole}_{1}(\Phi)\sim\mu_{\Phi}\,.

(31.1.13)

31.2

Dual theory

The mass spectra in the Seiberg dual IR free and logarithmically weakly coupled
at
\mu<\Lambda_{Q}

SU({\overline{N}}_{c})
theory (
30.0.2
) were described for this case in
section 7.1 of
[
26
]
. For the reader convenience and completeness we
reproduce here the results.

1) All
N_{F}^{2}
fions
\Phi^{j}_{i}
have large masses
\mu_{1}^{\rm pole}(\Phi)\sim\mu_{\Phi}\gg\Lambda_{Q}
(with logarithmic accuracy) and are dynamically
irrelevant at all lower scales.

2) All dual quarks
{\overline{q}}^{j},q_{i}
are in the overall Hq (heavy quark)
phase. There is a large number of hadrons made of weakly interacting,
non-relativistic and weakly confined dual quarks. The scale of their masses,
neglecting logarithmic RG-evolution factors, is
\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,L}\sim\langle M\rangle_{\rm L}/\Lambda_{Q}=({\overline{Q}}Q)_{\rm L}/\Lambda_{Q}\sim m^{\rm pole}_{Q,L}
, see
(
31.1.3
) (the tension of the confining string originating from
{\cal N}=1\,\,SU({\overline{N}}_{c})
SYM is much smaller,
\sqrt{\sigma}\sim\langle{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(L)}\rangle\ll\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,L}
, see
(
31.1.9
) ).

3) A large number of gluonia made of
SU({\overline{N}}_{c})
gluons with their mass scale

\sim\langle{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(L)}\rangle=\langle S\rangle_{L}^{1/3}\sim\Lambda_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q}/\mu_{\Phi})^{N_{F}/3(2N_{c}-N_{F})}
.

4)
N_{F}^{2}
Seiberg’s mions
M^{i}_{j}
with masses
\mu^{\rm pole}_{L}(M)\sim\Lambda_{Q}^{2}/\mu_{\Phi}
.

The mass hierarchies look here as
\mu^{\rm pole}_{L}(M)\ll\langle{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(L)}\rangle\ll\mu^{\rm pole}_{q}\ll\Lambda_{Q}\ll\mu^{\rm pole}_{1}(\Phi)\sim\mu_{\Phi}
.

Comparing the mass spectra of the direct and dual theories we note the
following.

1) The weakly confined dual quarks
{\overline{q}}^{j},q_{i}
inside dual hadrons are
non-relativistic and parametrically weakly coupled (the dual coupling
{\overline{a}}

at the scale of the Bohr momentum is logarithmically small). Therefore, the
Coulomb mass splittings of the low lying hadrons are also parametrically small,
i.e.
\delta M_{H}/M_{H}\sim{\overline{a}}^{\,2}\ll 1
. There is nothing similar in
the direct theory with the strongly coupled quarks
{\overline{Q}}_{j},Q^{i}
.

2) In the range of scales
\mu_{o}^{\rm str}\ll\mu\ll\Lambda_{Q}
the effectively
massless flavored particles in the direct theory are only quarks
{\overline{Q}}_{j},Q^{i}
, while all
N_{F}^{2}
fions
\Phi^{j}_{i}
have large running masses

\mu_{\Phi}(\mu)>\mu
and are dynamically irrelevant. In the dual theory the
effectively massless flavored particles are the dual quarks
{\overline{q}}^{j},q_{i}

and
N_{F}^{2}
mions
M^{i}_{j}
. Therefore, the anomalous ’t Hooft triangles

SU^{3}(N_{F})_{L}
are the same in the direct and dual theories
[
5
]
.

3) In the range of scales
\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,L}\sim m^{\rm pole}_{Q,L}\ll\mu\ll\mu_{o}^{\rm str}
the effectively massless flavored
particles in the dual theory remain the same, while
N_{F}^{2}
fions become
effectively massless (i.e. the running mass
\mu_{\Phi}(\mu)
of all fions is

\mu_{\Phi}(\mu)\ll\mu
) and give now additional contributions e.g. to

SU^{\,3}(N_{F})_{L}
triangles in the direct theory. Therefore, the

SU^{\,3}(N_{F})_{L}
triangles do not match now in the direct and dual theories.

4) At scales
\mu\ll m^{\rm pole}_{Q,L}\sim\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,L}
all direct and
dual quarks decouple. In the range of scales
\mu^{\rm pole}_{3,L}(\Phi)\sim\mu^{\rm pole}_{L}(M)\ll\mu\ll m^{\rm pole}_{Q,L}
the effectively massless
flavored particles in the direct theory are
N_{F}^{2}
third generation fions

\Phi^{j}_{i}
, while in the dual theory these are
N_{F}^{2}
mions
M_{j}^{i}
.
Therefore, the values of
SU^{\,3}(N_{F})_{L}
triangles differ in sign in the
direct and dual theories.

32

Unbroken flavor symmetry,  S-vacua

32.1

Direct theory

The quark condensates in these S (small)-vacua look as, see section 16,

\displaystyle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{S}\simeq-\frac{N_{c}}{{\overline{N}}_{c}}\,m_{Q}\mu_{\Phi}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\langle S\rangle_{S}=\Bigl(\frac{\det({\overline{Q}}Q)_{S}}{\Lambda_{Q}^{\rm\rm{b_{o}}}}\Bigr)^{1/{\overline{N}}_{c}}\sim\Lambda_{Q}^{3}\Bigl(\frac{m_{Q}\mu_{\Phi}}{\Lambda_{Q}^{2}}\Bigr)^{N_{F}/{\overline{N}}_{c}}\,.

(32.1.1)

The direct theory is strongly coupled at
\mu<\Lambda_{Q}
. Proceeding as in
section 31.1 we obtain for this S-vacuum, see
(
31.1.3
),(
31.1.4
),(
31.1.8
),(
32.1.1
),

\displaystyle\langle m_{Q}^{\rm tot}\rangle_{S}=\frac{\langle S\rangle_{S}}{({\overline{Q}}Q)_{S}}\sim\Lambda_{Q}\Bigl(\frac{({\overline{Q}}Q)_{S}}{\Lambda_{Q}^{2}}\Bigr)^{N_{c}/{\overline{N}}_{c}},\,\,m_{Q,S}^{\rm pole}=\frac{\langle m_{Q}^{\rm tot}\rangle_{S}}{z_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q},m_{Q,S}^{\rm pole})}\sim\frac{({\overline{Q}}Q)_{S}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\sim\frac{m_{Q}\mu_{\Phi}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\ll\Lambda_{Q}\,,

(32.1.2)

\Bigl(\mu^{\rm pole}_{{\rm gl},S}\Bigr)^{2}\sim a(\mu^{\rm pole}_{{\rm gl},S})z_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu^{\rm pole}_{{\rm gl},S})\,({\overline{Q}}Q)_{S}\sim\frac{\mu^{\rm pole}_{{\rm gl},S}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\,({\overline{Q}}Q)_{S}\hskip 11.74988pt\rightarrow\hskip 11.74988pt\mu^{\rm pole}_{{\rm gl},S}\sim\frac{({\overline{Q}}Q)_{S}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\sim\frac{m_{Q}\mu_{\Phi}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\sim m_{Q,S}^{\rm pole}\,.

Therefore, for the same reasons of the rank restrictions and unbroken global
flavor symmetry as in L-vacua in section 31.1, the overall phase is HQ.
Besides,

\displaystyle\frac{m_{Q,S}^{\rm pole}}{\mu_{o}^{\rm str}}\sim\frac{m_{Q}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigl(\frac{\mu_{\Phi}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\frac{2(2N_{c}-N_{F})}{5N_{c}-3N_{F}}}\ll\frac{m_{Q}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigl(\frac{m_{1}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\frac{2(2N_{c}-N_{F})}{5N_{c}-3N_{F}}}\sim\Bigl(\frac{m_{Q}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\frac{{\overline{N}}_{c}(3N_{c}-2N_{F})}{N_{c}(5N_{c}-3N_{F})}}\ll 1,\,\,\Lambda_{Q}\ll\mu_{\Phi}\ll m_{1},\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,

(32.1.3)

so that the running mass of all fions
\Phi^{j}_{i}
is
\mu_{\Phi}(\mu<\mu_{o}^{\rm str})<\mu
, all
N_{F}^{2}
fions become dynamically relevant at
\mu<\mu_{o}^{\rm str}
and there is the second generation of fions with
\mu_{2,S}^{\rm pole}\sim\mu_{o}^{\rm str}=\Lambda_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q}/\mu_{\Phi})^{{\overline{N}}_{c}/(5N_{c}-3N_{F})}\gg m_{Q,S}^{\rm pole}
. At
\mu<m_{Q,S}^{\rm pole}
all quarks decouple as heavy and,
proceeding   as in (
31.1.8
), we obtain the scale factor of remained

{\cal N}=1\,\,SU(N_{c})
SYM

\displaystyle a_{+}(\mu=m^{\rm pole}_{Q,S})=\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{m^{\rm pole}_{Q,S}}\Bigr)^{\nu_{Q}=(3N_{c}-2N_{F})/{\overline{N}}_{c}}=a^{\rm str}_{YM}(\mu=m^{\rm pole}_{Q,S})=\Bigl(\frac{\mu=m^{\rm pole}_{Q,S}}{\langle{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(S)}\rangle}\Bigr)^{3}\gg 1\,.

(32.1.4)

From (
32.1.2
),(
32.1.4
)

\langle{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(S)}\rangle^{3}\sim\Lambda_{Q}^{3}\Bigl(\frac{\langle m_{Q}^{\rm pole}\rangle_{S}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{N_{F}/{\overline{N}}_{c}}\sim\Lambda_{Q}^{3}\Bigl(\frac{m_{Q}\mu_{\Phi}}{\Lambda_{Q}^{2}}\Bigr)^{N_{F}/{\overline{N}}_{c}},

\displaystyle\frac{\langle{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(S)}\rangle}{m^{\rm pole}_{Q,S}}\sim\Bigl(\frac{m_{Q}\mu_{\Phi}}{\Lambda_{Q}^{2}}\Bigr)^{(3N_{c}-2N_{F})/3{\overline{N}}_{c}}\ll\Bigl(\frac{m_{Q}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{(3N_{c}-2N_{F})/3N_{c}}\ll 1,\,\,\,

(32.1.5)

as it should be because, see (
32.1.1
),

\displaystyle\langle{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(S)}\rangle^{3}\equiv\langle S\rangle_{S}=\Biggl(\frac{\det\langle{\bar{Q}}Q\rangle_{S}}{\Lambda_{Q}^{\rm\rm{b_{o}}}}\Biggr)^{1/{\overline{N}}_{c}}\sim\Lambda_{Q}^{3}\Bigl(\frac{m_{Q}\mu_{\Phi}}{\Lambda_{Q}^{2}}\Bigr)^{N_{F}/{\overline{N}}_{c}}\,.

(32.1.6)

The low energy Lagrangian at
\mu<\langle{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(S)}\rangle
has
the same form as in (
31.1.10
), but now in S - vacua. In this
case the main contribution to masses of third generation fions
originates from the nonperturbative term in(
31.1.10
) and is

\displaystyle\mu^{\rm pole}_{3,S}(\Phi)\sim\frac{1}{z_{\Phi}(\Lambda_{Q},m_{Q,S}^{\rm pole})}\,\frac{\langle S\rangle_{S}}{\langle m_{Q,S}^{\rm tot}\rangle^{2}}\sim\Lambda_{Q}\Bigl(\frac{m_{Q}\mu_{\Phi}}{\Lambda_{Q}^{2}}\Bigr)^{(2N_{c}-N_{F})/{\overline{N}}_{c}}\,,

(32.1.7)

\displaystyle\frac{\mu^{\rm pole}_{3,S}(\Phi)}{\langle{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(S)}\rangle}\sim\Bigl(\frac{m_{Q}\mu_{\Phi}}{\Lambda_{Q}^{2}}\Bigr)^{2(3N_{c}-2N_{F})/3{\overline{N}}_{c}}<\Bigl(\frac{m_{Q}m_{1}}{\Lambda_{Q}^{2}}\Bigr)^{2(3N_{c}-2N_{F})3{\overline{N}}_{c}}\sim\Bigl(\frac{m_{Q}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{2(3N_{c}-2N_{F})/3N_{c}}\ll 1.\,\,\,

(32.1.8)

The overall mass hierarchies look as

\displaystyle\mu^{\rm pole}_{3,S}(\Phi)\ll\langle{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(S)}\rangle\ll m^{\rm pole}_{Q,S}\ll\mu^{\rm pole}_{2,S}(\Phi)\sim\mu_{o}^{\rm str}\ll\Lambda_{Q}\ll\mu_{1}^{\rm pole}(\Phi)\sim\mu_{\Phi}\,.

(32.1.9)

32.2

Dual theory

The mass spectra in the Seiberg dual
SU({\overline{N}}_{c})
theory for this case were also
described in section 7.2 of
[
26
]
. The results look as follows.

1) All
N_{F}^{2}
fions
\Phi^{j}_{i}
have large masses
\mu_{1}^{\rm pole}(\Phi)\sim\mu_{\Phi}\gg\Lambda_{Q}
(with logarithmic accuracy) and are dynamically
irrelevant at all lower scales.

2) All dual quarks
{\overline{q}}^{j},q_{i}
are in the overall Hq (heavy quark)
phase. There is a large number of hadrons made of weakly interacting
non-relativistic and weakly confined dual quarks, the scale of their masses
(with logarithmic accuracy) is
\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,S}\sim\langle M\rangle_{\rm S}/\Lambda_{Q}=({\overline{Q}}Q)_{\rm S}/\Lambda_{Q}\sim(m_{Q}\mu_{\Phi})/\Lambda_{Q}\sim m^{\rm pole}_{Q,S}
, see
(
31.1.3
),(
32.1.1
)  (the tension of the confining string
originating
from
{\cal N}=1\,\,SU({\overline{N}}_{c})
SYM is much smaller,
\sqrt{\sigma}\sim\langle{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(S)}\rangle\ll\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,S}\sim m^{\rm pole}_{Q,S}
).

3) A large number of gluonia made of
SU({\overline{N}}_{c})
gluons with their mass scale

\sim\langle{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(S)}\rangle=\langle S\rangle_{S}^{1/3}\sim\Lambda_{Q}(m_{Q}\mu_{\Phi}/\Lambda_{Q}^{2})^{N_{F}/3{\overline{N}}_{c}}
.

4)
N_{F}^{2}
Seiberg’s mions
M^{i}_{j}
with masses
\mu^{\rm pole}_{S}(M)\sim\Lambda_{Q}^{2}/\mu_{\Phi}
.

The hierarchies of masses (except for
\mu^{\rm pole}_{1}(\Phi)\sim\mu_{\Phi}\gg\Lambda_{Q}
)
look here as:

a)
\Lambda_{Q}\gg\mu^{\rm pole}_{S}(M)\gg\mu^{\rm pole}_{q}\gg{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(\rm S)}
at

\Lambda_{Q}\ll\mu_{\Phi}\ll{\mu_{\Phi}^{\prime}}=\Lambda_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q}/m_{Q})^{1/2}\,
;

b)
\Lambda_{Q}\gg\mu^{\rm pole}_{q}\gg\mu^{\rm pole}_{S}(M)\gg{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(\rm S)}
at

{\mu_{\Phi}^{\prime}}\ll\mu_{\Phi}\ll{\tilde{\mu}}_{\Phi}=\Lambda_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q}/m_{Q})^{N_{F}/(4N_{F}-3N_{c})}\,
;

c)
\Lambda_{Q}\gg\mu^{\rm pole}_{q}\gg{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(\rm S)}\gg\mu^{\rm pole}_{S}(M)
at

{\tilde{\mu}}_{\Phi}\ll\mu_{\Phi}\ll m_{1}=\Lambda_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q}/m_{Q})^{(2N_{c}-N_{F})/N_{c}}\,
.

It is seen that, at least,
\mu^{\rm pole}_{3,S}(\Phi)\sim\Lambda_{Q}\Bigl(m_{Q}\mu_{\Phi}/\Lambda_{Q}^{2}\Bigr)^{(2N_{c}-N_{F})/{\overline{N}}_{c}}
(
32.1.7
) in the direct theory
and

\mu^{\rm pole}_{S}(M)\sim\Lambda_{Q}^{2}/\mu_{\Phi}
in the dual one are parametrically
different (the ’t Hooft triangles
SU^{3}(N_{F})_{L}
are also different).

33

Broken flavor symmetry,   L-type vacua

The main qualitative difference compared with the L-vacua in section 2 is the
spontaneous breaking of the flavor symmetry,
U(N_{F})\rightarrow U(n_{1})\times U(n_{2})
,
see section 16,

\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{Lt}\equiv\langle{\overline{Q}}_{1}Q^{1}\rangle_{Lt}=\langle M_{1}\rangle_{Lt}\neq\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{Lt}=\langle{\overline{Q}}_{2}Q^{2}\rangle_{Lt}=\langle M_{2}\rangle_{Lt}\,,

\displaystyle(1-\frac{{\rm n}_{1}}{N_{c}})\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{Lt}\simeq-\,(1-\frac{{\rm n}_{2}}{N_{c}})\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{Lt}\sim({\overline{Q}}Q)_{L}\sim\Lambda_{Q}^{2}\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\Bigr)^{{\overline{N}}_{c}/(2N_{c}-N_{F})}\ll\Lambda_{Q}^{2}\,.

(33.0.1)

For this reason, unlike the L-vacua, the fions
\Phi^{1}_{2},\,\Phi^{2}_{1}
in the
direct theory and mions
M^{1}_{2},\,M^{2}_{1}
in the dual one are the
Nambu-Goldstone   particles and are massless. Except for this, all other masses in
these Lt-vacua of the direct and dual theories are parametrically the same as in
L-vacua. Therefore, all differences between the direct and dual theories
described above for the L-vacua remain in L-type vacua also.

34

Broken flavor symmetry,   br2 vacua

34.1

Direct theory

The quark condensates of the
SU(N_{c})
theory look in these vacua with

{\rm n}_{2}>N_{c},\,{\rm n}_{1}<{\overline{N}}_{c}
as,

\displaystyle\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{\rm br2}\simeq\frac{N_{c}}{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{2}}m_{Q}\mu_{\Phi}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{\rm br2}\sim\Lambda_{Q}^{2}\Bigl(\frac{m_{Q}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\frac{N_{c}-n_{1}}{n_{2}-N_{c}}}\Bigl(\frac{\mu_{\Phi}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\frac{n_{2}}{n_{2}-N_{c}}}\,,

(34.1.1)

\frac{\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{\rm br2}}{\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{\rm br2}}\sim\Bigl(\frac{\mu_{\Phi}}{m_{1}}\Bigr)^{\frac{N_{c}}{n_{2}-N_{c}}}\ll 1\,,\hskip 11.74988ptm_{1}=\Lambda_{Q}\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{m_{Q}}\Bigr)^{(2N_{c}-N_{F})/N_{c}}\,,

\langle{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(\rm br2)}\rangle^{3}\equiv\langle S\rangle_{\rm br2}=\Bigl(\frac{\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{\rm br2}^{{\rm n}_{1}}\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{\rm br2}^{{\rm n}_{2}}}{\Lambda_{Q}^{3N_{c}-N_{F}}}\Bigr)^{1/{\overline{N}}_{c}}\sim\Lambda_{Q}^{3}\Bigl(\frac{m_{Q}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\frac{n_{2}-n_{1}}{n_{2}-N_{c}}}\Bigl(\frac{\mu_{\Phi}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\frac{n_{2}}{n_{2}-N_{c}}}\,.

From (
34.1.1
) and the Konishi anomalies,

\langle m_{Q,1}^{\rm tot}\rangle_{\rm br2}=m_{Q}-\langle\Phi_{1}\rangle_{\rm br2}=\frac{\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{\rm br2}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\sim m_{Q}\gg\langle m_{Q,2}^{\rm tot}\rangle_{\rm br2}=\frac{\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{\rm br2}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\sim\Bigl(\frac{m_{Q}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\frac{N_{c}-n_{1}}{n_{2}-N_{c}}}\Bigl(\frac{\mu_{\Phi}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\frac{N_{c}}{n_{2}-N_{c}}},

\displaystyle m_{Q,1}^{\rm pole}=\frac{\langle m_{Q,1}^{\rm tot}\rangle_{\rm br2}}{z_{Q}^{+}(\Lambda_{Q},m_{Q,1}^{\rm pole})}\sim\Lambda_{Q}\Bigl(\frac{m_{Q}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{{\overline{N}}_{c}/N_{c}},\,\,z_{Q}^{+}(\Lambda_{Q},m_{Q,1}^{\rm pole})=\Bigl(\frac{m_{Q,1}^{\rm pole}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\gamma_{Q}^{+}}\,\,\gamma_{Q}^{+}=\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{N_{F}-N_{c}}>1,\,\,\,

(34.1.2)

while the gluon mass from the possible higgsing of
{\overline{Q}}_{2},Q^{2}
quarks
looks here as, see (
31.1.4
),

\Bigl({\mu}^{\rm pole}_{gl,2}\Bigr)^{2}\sim\Biggl[a_{+}(\mu={\mu}_{gl,2})=\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{{\mu}^{\rm pole}_{gl,2}}\Bigr)^{\nu^{+}_{Q}}\Biggr]z_{Q}^{+}(\Lambda_{Q},{\mu}^{\rm pole}_{gl,2})\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{\rm br2}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\nu^{+}_{Q}=\gamma^{+}_{Q}-1=\frac{3N_{c}-2N_{F}}{N_{F}-N_{c}}>\,0\,,

\displaystyle a_{+}({\mu}_{gl,2})\,z_{Q}^{+}(\Lambda_{Q},{\mu}^{\rm pole}_{gl,2})\sim\frac{{\mu}^{\rm pole}_{gl,2}}{\Lambda_{Q}},\,\,{\mu}^{\rm pole}_{gl,2}\sim\frac{\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{\rm br2}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\sim\frac{m_{Q}\mu_{\Phi}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\gg\mu_{gl,1}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\frac{{\mu}^{\rm pole}_{gl,2}}{m_{Q,1}^{\rm pole}}\sim\frac{\mu_{\Phi}}{m_{1}}\ll 1.\,\,\,\,

(34.1.3)

Besides, see (
31.1.7
), because the largest mass in the quark-gluon
sector
is
m_{Q,1}^{\rm pole}
and

\displaystyle\frac{m_{Q,1}^{\rm pole}}{\mu_{o}^{\rm str}}\sim\Bigl(\frac{m_{Q}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\frac{{\overline{N}}_{c}}{N_{c}}}\Bigl(\frac{\mu_{\Phi}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\frac{{\overline{N}}_{c}}{5N_{c}-3N_{F}}}\ll\Bigl(\frac{m_{Q}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\frac{{\overline{N}}_{c}}{N_{c}}}\Bigl(\frac{m_{1}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\frac{{\overline{N}}_{c}}{5N_{c}-3N_{F}}}\sim\Bigl(\frac{m_{Q}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\frac{{\overline{N}}_{c}(3N_{c}-2N_{F})}{N_{c}(5N_{c}-3N_{F})}\,>0}\ll 1,\,\,\,\,\,

(34.1.4)

\mu_{o}^{\rm str}=\Lambda_{Q}\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\Bigr)^{\frac{1}{2\gamma^{+}_{Q}-1}}=\Lambda_{Q}\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\Bigr)^{\frac{{\overline{N}}_{c}}{5N_{c}-3N_{F}}}\ll\Lambda_{Q}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt{\overline{N}}_{c}=N_{F}-N_{c}\,,

there are
N_{F}^{2}
of 2-nd generation fions
\Phi^{j}_{i}
with masses
\mu_{2}^{\rm pole}(\Phi)\sim\mu_{o}^{\rm str}=\Lambda_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q}/\mu_{\Phi})^{{\overline{N}}_{c}/(5N_{c}-3N_{F})}\gg m_{Q,1}^{\rm pole}
.

After the heaviest quarks
{\overline{Q}}_{1},Q^{1}
decouple at
\mu<m_{Q,1}^{\rm pole}
, the RG evolution of
\Phi^{1}_{1},\Phi^{2}_{1},\Phi^{1}_{2}
fions is frozen,
while the new quantum numbers are

\displaystyle N_{F}^{\prime}=N_{F}-n_{1}=n_{2},\,\,N_{c}^{\prime}=N_{c},\,\,\gamma_{Q}^{-}=\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}+n_{1}}{n_{2}-N_{c}}>\gamma_{Q}^{+},\,\,\nu^{-}_{Q}=\frac{3N_{c}-2N_{F}+2n_{1}}{n_{2}-N_{c}}>\nu^{+}_{Q}.\,\,\,\,

(34.1.5)

From (
34.1.5
), the pole mass of
{\overline{Q}}_{2},Q^{2}
quarks is, see
(
34.1.2
),

\displaystyle m_{Q,2}^{\rm pole}=\frac{\langle m_{Q,2}^{\rm tot}\rangle_{\rm br2}}{z_{Q}^{+}(\Lambda_{Q},m_{Q,1}^{\rm pole})z_{Q}^{-}(m_{Q,1}^{\rm pole},m_{Q,2}^{\rm pole})}\sim\frac{m_{Q}\mu_{\Phi}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\,,

(34.1.6)

z_{Q}^{-}(m_{Q,1}^{\rm pole},m_{Q,2}^{\rm pole})=\Bigl(\frac{m_{Q,2}^{\rm pole}}{m_{Q,1}^{\rm pole}}\Bigr)^{\gamma_{Q}^{-}}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\frac{m_{Q,2}^{\rm pole}}{m_{Q,1}^{\rm pole}}\sim\frac{\mu_{\Phi}}{m_{1}}\ll 1\,.

Besides, because
n_{2}>N_{c}
in these br2-vacua, the quarks
{\overline{Q}}_{2},Q^{2}
are
not higgsed due to the rank restriction, as otherwise the flavor
symmetry
U(n_{2})
will be further broken spontaneously. Therefore, the
overall phase is
HQ_{1}-HQ_{2}
. All quarks are not higgsed but weakly
confined, the confinement originates from
{\cal N}=1\,\,SU(N_{c})
SYM, so
that the scale of the confining string is
\sqrt{\sigma}\sim\langle{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(\rm br2)}\rangle\ll m_{Q,2}^{\rm pole}
, see (
34.1.1
),(
34.1.6
).

After the quarks
{\overline{Q}}_{2},Q^{2}
decouple at
\mu<m_{Q,2}^{\rm pole}
, there
remain
{\cal N}=1\,\,SU(N_{c})
SYM and
N_{F}^{2}
fions
\Phi^{j}_{i}
. The value of
the scale factor
\langle{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{\rm(br2)}\rangle
of SYM is determined from the
matching

a_{-}(\mu=m_{Q,2}^{\rm pole})=\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{m_{Q,1}^{\rm pole}}\Bigr)^{\nu^{+}_{Q}}\Bigl(\frac{m_{Q,1}^{\rm pole}}{m_{Q,2}^{\rm pole}}\Bigr)^{\nu^{-}_{Q}}=a^{\rm str}_{YM}(\mu=m_{Q,2}^{\rm pole})=\Bigl(\frac{m_{Q,2}^{\rm pole}}{\langle{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{\rm(br2)}\rangle}\Bigr)^{3}\hskip 11.74988pt\rightarrow

\displaystyle\hskip 11.74988pt\rightarrow\hskip 11.74988pt\Bigl(\langle{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{\rm(br2)}\rangle\Bigr)^{3}\sim\Lambda_{Q}^{3}\Bigl(\frac{m_{Q}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\frac{n_{2}-n_{1}}{n_{2}-N_{c}}}\Bigl(\frac{\mu_{\Phi}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\frac{n_{2}}{n_{2}-N_{c}}}\,,

(34.1.7)

as it should be, see (
34.1.1
),

\frac{\langle{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{\rm(br2)}\rangle}{m_{Q,2}^{\rm pole}}\sim\Bigl(\frac{m_{Q}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\frac{3N_{c}-2N_{F}+n_{1}}{3(n_{2}-N_{c})}}\Bigl(\frac{\mu_{\Phi}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\frac{3N_{c}-2N_{F}+2n_{1}}{3(n_{2}-N_{c})}}\ll\Bigl(\frac{m_{Q}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\frac{3N_{c}-2N_{F}+n_{1}}{3(n_{2}-N_{c})}}\Bigl(\frac{m_{1}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\frac{3N_{c}-2N_{F}+2n_{1}}{3(n_{2}-N_{c})}}\sim

\sim\Bigl(\frac{m_{Q}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\frac{(3N_{c}-2N_{F})({\overline{N}}_{c}-n_{1})}{3N_{c}(n_{2}-N_{c})}\,>\,0}\ll 1\,,\hskip 11.74988ptn_{1}<{\overline{N}}_{c}\,,\hskip 11.74988ptn_{2}>N_{c}\,.

After integrating out all gluons at
\mu<\langle{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{\rm(br2)}\rangle
via
the   VY procedure
[
15
]
, the lower energy Lagrangian of
N_{F}^{2}
fions

\Phi^{j}_{i}
looks as, see (
30.0.1
) for
{\cal W}_{\Phi}
and
(
34.1.3
),(
34.1.6
),

\displaystyle K_{\Phi}=z_{\Phi}^{+}(\Lambda_{Q},m_{Q,1}^{\rm pole})\,{\rm Tr}\,\Bigl[(\Phi^{1}_{1})^{\dagger}\Phi^{1}_{1}+\Bigl((\Phi^{1}_{2})^{\dagger}\Phi^{1}_{2}+(\Phi^{2}_{1})^{\dagger}\Phi^{2}_{1})+z_{\Phi}^{-}(m_{Q,1}^{\rm pole},m_{Q,2}^{\rm pole})\,(\Phi^{2}_{2})^{\dagger}\Phi^{2}_{2}\Bigr],

(34.1.8)

{\cal W}={\cal W}_{\Phi}+{\cal W}_{\rm{non-pert}},\hskip 11.74988pt{\cal W}_{\rm{non-pert}}=N_{c}\Bigl(\Lambda_{Q}^{\rm\rm{b_{o}}}\det m_{Q}^{\rm tot}\Bigr)^{1/N_{c}},\hskip 11.74988ptm_{Q}^{\rm tot}=(m_{Q}-\Phi)\,.

From (
34.1.8
), the masses of the third generation fions look as, see
(
34.1.1
),(
34.1.2
),(
34.1.6
),

\displaystyle\mu^{\rm pole}_{3}(\Phi_{1}^{1})\sim\frac{\mu_{\Phi}}{z_{\Phi}^{+}(\Lambda_{Q},m_{Q,1}^{\rm pole})}=\mu_{\Phi}\Bigl(z_{Q}^{+}(\Lambda_{Q},m_{Q,1}^{\rm pole})\Bigr)^{2}\sim\mu_{\Phi}\Bigl(\frac{m_{Q}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\frac{2(2N_{c}-N_{F})}{N_{c}}}\,,

(34.1.9)

z_{\Phi}^{\pm}(\mu_{1},\mu_{2})=(\frac{\mu_{2}}{\mu_{1}})^{\gamma_{\Phi}^{\pm}\,=\,-2\gamma_{Q}^{\pm}}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\frac{\mu^{\rm pole}_{3}(\Phi_{1}^{1})}{m_{Q,1}^{\rm pole}}\ll\frac{\mu^{\rm pole}_{3}(\Phi_{1}^{1})}{m_{Q,2}^{\rm pole}}\sim\Bigl(\frac{m_{Q}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\frac{3N_{c}-2N_{F}}{N_{c}}}\ll 1\,,

\displaystyle\mu^{\rm pole}_{3}(\Phi_{2}^{2})\sim\frac{\langle S\rangle_{\rm br2}}{\langle m_{Q,2}^{\rm tot}\rangle_{\rm br2}^{2}}\,\frac{1}{z_{\Phi}^{+}(\Lambda_{Q},m_{Q,1}^{\rm pole})z_{\Phi}^{-}(m_{Q,1}^{\rm pole},m_{Q,2}^{\rm pole})}\sim\frac{\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{\rm br2}}{\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{\rm br2}}\,\frac{\mu^{\rm pole}_{3}(\Phi_{1}^{1})}{z_{\Phi}^{-}(m_{Q,1}^{\rm pole},m_{Q,2}^{\rm pole})}\,,

(34.1.10)

\mu^{\rm pole}_{3}(\Phi_{2}^{2})\sim\Lambda_{Q}\Bigl(\frac{m_{Q}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{n_{2}-N_{c}}}\Bigl(\frac{\mu_{\Phi}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}+n_{1}}{n_{2}-N_{c}}},\hskip 11.74988pt\hskip 11.74988pt\frac{\mu^{\rm pole}_{3}(\Phi_{2}^{2})}{\langle{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{\rm(br2)}\rangle}\ll 1\,,

(the main contribution to
\mu^{\rm pole}_{3}(\Phi_{1}^{1})
originates from the
term
{\cal W}_{\Phi}\sim\mu_{\Phi}{\rm Tr}\,(\Phi^{2})
in (
34.1.8
), while
the   main contribution to
\mu^{\rm pole}_{3}(\Phi_{2}^{2})
originates from
{\cal W}_{\rm non-pert}
in (
34.1.8
) ).

The fions
\Phi_{1}^{2}
and
\Phi^{1}_{2}
are the Nambu-Goldstone particles and are
massless.

On the whole for this case the mass spectrum looks as follows.

1) Among the masses smaller than
\Lambda_{Q}
, the largest are the masses of
N_{F}^{2}

2-nd generation fions,

\mu^{\rm pole}_{2}(\Phi)\sim\mu_{o}^{\rm str}\sim\Lambda_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q}/\mu_{\Phi})^{{\overline{N}}_{c}/(5N_{c}-3N_{F})}
.

2) Next are masses of
{\overline{Q}}_{1},Q^{1}
quarks,
m_{Q,1}^{\rm pole}\sim\Lambda_{Q}(m_{Q}/\Lambda_{Q})^{{\overline{N}}_{c}/N_{c}}
, see (
34.1.2
).

3) The masses of
{\overline{Q}}_{2},Q^{2}
quarks are
m_{Q,2}^{\rm pole}\sim m_{Q}\mu_{\Phi}/\Lambda_{Q}\ll m_{Q,1}^{\rm pole}
, see (
34.1.6
).

4) There is a large number of
SU(N_{c})
SYM gluonia with the mass scale

\sim\langle{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{\rm(br2)}\rangle\ll m_{Q,2}^{\rm pole}
, see
(
34.1.7
) .

5) The 3-rd generation fions
\Phi_{1}^{1}
have masses
\mu^{\rm pole}_{3}(\Phi_{1}^{1})\sim\mu_{\Phi}(m_{Q}/\Lambda_{Q})^{2(2N_{c}-N_{F})/N_{c}}\ll m_{Q,1}^{\rm pole}
, see (
34.1.9
).

6) The 3-rd generation fions
\Phi_{2}^{2}
have masses (
34.1.10
),

\mu^{\rm pole}_{3}(\Phi_{2}^{2})\ll\langle{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{\rm(br2)}\rangle
.

7) The 3-rd generation fions
\Phi_{1}^{2},\Phi_{2}^{1}
are the massless
Nambu-Goldstone particles.

The overall hierarchy of nonzero masses look as

\mu^{\rm pole}_{3}(\Phi_{2}^{2})\ll\langle{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{\rm(br2)}\rangle\ll m_{Q,2}^{\rm pole}\ll m_{Q,1}^{\rm pole}\ll\mu^{\rm pole}_{2}(\Phi)\ll\Lambda_{Q}\ll\mu_{1}^{\rm pole}(\Phi)\sim\mu_{\Phi},

\mu^{\rm pole}_{3}(\Phi_{2}^{2})\ll\mu^{\rm pole}_{3}(\Phi_{1}^{1})\ll m_{Q,2}^{\rm pole}\ll m_{Q,1}^{\rm pole}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\Lambda_{Q}\ll\mu_{\Phi}\ll m_{1}=\Lambda_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q}/m_{Q})^{(2N_{c}-N_{F})/N_{c}}.

Let us point out also the following, see section 8.1 in
[
25
]
. The dual

SU(N_{c})
theory in br1-vacua considered therein looks as (see eq.(8.1.10) in

[
25
]
)

K={\rm Tr}\,(\Phi^{\dagger}\Phi)+\frac{1}{\tilde{\Lambda}^{2}}{\rm Tr}\,(M^{\dagger}M)+{\rm Tr}\,({\textsf{q}}^{\dagger}\textsf{q}+{\overline{\textsf{q}}}^{\dagger}{\overline{\textsf{q}}}),\hskip 11.74988pt\widetilde{m}=\frac{N_{c}}{{\overline{N}}_{c}}\,m\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\widetilde{\mu}_{\rm x}=-\mu_{\rm x}\,,\hskip 11.74988ptm\ll\mu_{\rm x}\ll\Lambda_{2}\,,

W_{\rm matter}={\cal W}_{\Phi}+{\rm Tr}\,\,(\widetilde{m}-\Phi)M\,-\frac{1}{\tilde{\Lambda}}{\rm Tr}\,(\,{\overline{\textsf{q}}}\,M\textsf{q}\,)\,,\hskip 11.74988pt{\cal W}_{\Phi}=\frac{\widetilde{\mu}_{\rm x}}{2}\Bigl(\,{\rm Tr}\,(\Phi^{2})-\frac{1}{N_{c}}({\rm Tr}\,\Phi)^{2}\Bigr)\,.

Let us compare now the mass spectra of this theory with those of
(
30.0.1
),
with the substitution (see eq.(8.1.24) in
[
25
]
, both these
SU(N_{c})

theories with
N_{c}<N_{F}<3N_{c}/2
quark flavors are strongly coupled at
scales
\mu<\Lambda_{Q}
):

\displaystyle\Lambda_{Q}=-\tilde{\Lambda}=(\Lambda_{2})\Biggl(\frac{\Lambda_{2}}{\mu_{\rm x}}\Biggr)^{\frac{{\overline{N}}_{c}}{3N_{c}-2N_{F}}},\hskip 11.74988ptm_{Q}=m\,\frac{\tilde{\Lambda}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\mu_{\Phi}=\frac{\tilde{\Lambda}^{2}}{\mu_{\rm x}}\,,\hskip 11.74988ptm\ll\mu_{\rm x}\ll\Lambda_{2}\,.

(34.1.11)

The particle masses smaller than
\mu_{\Phi}
look then as follows :

a) the masses
m^{\rm pole}_{Q,1}
of
{\overline{Q}}_{1},Q^{1}
quarks, see
(
34.1.2
), compare with eq.(8.1.11) in
[
25
]
,

m^{\rm pole}_{Q,1}\sim\Lambda_{Q}\Bigl(\frac{m_{Q}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\frac{{\overline{N}}_{c}}{N_{c}}}\sim\Lambda_{2}(\frac{m}{\Lambda_{2}})^{{\frac{{\overline{N}}_{c}}{N_{c}}}}\sim\mu_{\textsf{q},1}^{\rm pole}\,,\hskip 256.0748pt

b) the masses
m^{\rm pole}_{Q,2}
of
{\overline{Q}}_{2},Q^{2}
quarks, see
(
34.1.6
), compare with eq.(8.1.11) in
[
25
]
,

m_{Q,2}^{\rm pole}\sim\frac{m_{Q}\mu_{\Phi}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\sim m\sim\mu_{\textsf{q},2}^{\rm pole}\,,\hskip 327.20668pt

c) the mass scale of gluonia, see (
34.1.7
), compare with
eq.(8.1.8), eq.(8.1.9) in
[
25
]
,

\langle{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{\rm(br2)}\rangle^{3}\sim\Lambda_{Q}^{3}\Bigl(\frac{m_{Q}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\frac{n_{2}-n_{1}}{n_{2}-N_{c}}}\Bigl(\frac{\mu_{\Phi}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\frac{n_{2}}{n_{2}-N_{c}}}\sim\mu_{\rm x}m^{2}\Bigl(\frac{m}{\Lambda_{2}}\Bigr)^{\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{{\rm n}_{2}-N_{c}}}\sim\langle{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{\rm(br1)}\rangle^{3}\,,\,\hskip 99.58464pt

d) The masses of 3-rd generation fions
\Phi_{1}^{1}
, see (
34.1.9
),
compare  with eq.8.1.16) in
[
25
]
,

\mu^{\rm pole}_{3}(\Phi_{1}^{1})\sim\mu_{\Phi}\Bigl(\frac{m_{Q}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\frac{2(2N_{c}-N_{F})}{N_{c}}}\sim\mu_{\rm x}\Bigl(\frac{m}{\Lambda_{2}}\Bigr)^{\frac{2(2N_{c}-N_{F})}{N_{c}}}\sim\mu^{\rm pole}({M}_{1}^{1})\,,\hskip 142.26378pt

e) The masses of 3-rd generation fions
\Phi_{2}^{2}
, see (
34.1.10
),
compare  with eq.(8.1.17) in
[
25
]
,

\mu^{\rm pole}_{3}(\Phi_{2}^{2})\sim\Lambda_{Q}\Bigl(\frac{m_{Q}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{n_{2}-N_{c}}}\Bigl(\frac{\mu_{\Phi}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}+n_{1}}{n_{2}-N_{c}}}\sim\mu_{\rm x}\Bigl(\frac{m}{\Lambda_{2}}\Bigr)^{\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{n_{2}-N_{c}}}\sim\mu^{\rm pole}({M}_{2}^{2})\,,\hskip 79.6678pt

f) The hybrids
\Phi^{1}_{2}
and
M^{1}_{2}
are massless.

Therefore, with the choice of parameters as in (
34.1.11
) and with
correspondences
Q\leftrightarrow\textsf{q}
and
\Phi\leftrightarrow M
,
the mass spectra coincide in the direct
SU(N_{c})
theory
(

) (this is the direct theory eq.(8.1.18) in
[

]
) and in
the dual
SU(N_{c})
theory eq.(8.1.10) in
[
25
]
. Both are strongly
coupled at
N_{c}<N_{F}<3N_{c}/2

\,SU(N_{c})
theories. Much more details
can be found in sections 8.1-8.3 of
[
