]
.

34.2

Dual theory

For the reader convenience and to make this article self-contained, we
reproduce below in short the results from the section 7.3 in
[
26
]
for
the   mass spectra of the weakly coupled dual theory (
30.0.2
) (with
(
34.1.11
),
the spectrum of masses in the case ”A”  below is the same
as in the theories eq.(8.1.7) and eq.(8.1.19) in
[
25
]
).

A) The range
\Lambda_{Q}\ll\mu_{\Phi}\ll\Lambda_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q}/m_{Q})^{1/2}

The overall phase is
Higgs_{1}-Hq_{2}
in this range due to
higgsing of dual quarks
q_{1},{\overline{q}}^{1}
,
SU({\overline{N}}_{c})\rightarrow SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-n_{1})
. On the whole
for this case the mass spectrum looks as follows.

1) The heaviest (among the masses
<\Lambda_{Q}
) are
N_{F}^{2}
mions
M^{i}_{j}
with
masses
\mu^{\rm pole}(M)\sim\Lambda_{Q}^{2}/\mu_{\Phi}\,
.

2) There are
n_{1}(2{\overline{N}}_{c}-n_{1})
massive dual gluons (and their super-partners)
with masses

{\overline{\mu}}^{\,\rm pole}_{\rm gl,1}\sim\langle N_{1}\rangle^{1/2}\sim(m_{Q}\Lambda_{Q})^{1/2}
.

3) There is a large number of hadrons made of weakly interacting
non-relativistic and weakly confined dual quarks
q^{\,\prime}_{2}
and
{\overline{q}}^{\,\prime,\,2}
with unbroken colors, the scale of their masses is

\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,2}\sim m_{Q}\mu_{\Phi}/\Lambda_{Q}\sim m_{Q,2}^{\rm pole}
, see
(
34.1.6
),   (the tension of the confining string is much smaller,
\sqrt{\sigma}\sim\langle{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(\rm br2)}\rangle\ll\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,2}
, see
(
34.1.7
)).

4) The masses of
n_{1}^{2}
nions
N_{1}^{1}
(dual pions) are also
\mu^{\rm pole}(N_{1}^{1})\sim m_{Q}\mu_{\Phi}/\Lambda_{Q}
.

5) There is a large number of
SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-n_{1})
SYM gluonia, the scale of their
masses is

\sim\langle{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(\rm br2)}\rangle\sim(m_{Q}\langle M_{1}\rangle_{\rm br2})^{1/3}=(m_{Q}\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{\rm br2})^{1/3}
, see
(
34.1.1
),(
34.1.7
).

6) Finally,
2n_{1}n_{2}
Nambu-Goldstone hybrid nions
N_{1}^{2},N_{2}^{1}
are
massless. The overall hierarchy of nonzero masses looks as:

\displaystyle\langle{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(\rm br2)}\rangle\ll\mu(N_{1}^{1})\sim\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,2}\ll{\overline{\mu}}_{\rm gl,1}\ll\mu^{\rm pole}(M)\ll\Lambda_{Q}\,.

(34.2.1)

B) The range
\Lambda_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q}/m_{Q})^{1/2}\ll\mu_{\Phi}\ll m_{1}=\Lambda_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q}/m_{Q})^{(2N_{c}-N_{F})/N_{c}}

The largest mass have in this case
q_{2},{\overline{q}}^{\,2}
quarks,

\displaystyle\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,2}\sim\frac{\langle M_{2}\rangle_{\rm br2}}{\Lambda_{Q}}=\frac{\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{\rm br2}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\sim\frac{m_{Q}\mu_{\Phi}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\,.

(34.2.2)

After integrating them out at
\mu<\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,2}
, the new
scale factor of the gauge coupling is

\displaystyle\Bigl(\Lambda^{\prime\prime}\Bigr)^{3{\overline{N}}_{c}-n_{1}}=\Lambda_{Q}^{\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}}\Bigl(\frac{m_{Q}\mu_{\Phi}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{n_{2}},\hskip 11.74988pt\Lambda^{\prime\prime}\ll\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,2}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}=3{\overline{N}}_{c}-N_{F}\,,\hskip 11.74988ptn_{1}<{\overline{N}}_{c}\,.

(34.2.3)

In the range
(\Lambda_{Q}^{3}/m_{Q})^{1/2}\ll\mu_{\Phi}\ll{\mu}_{\Phi}^{\prime\prime}=\Lambda_{Q}\Bigl(\Lambda_{Q}/m_{Q}\Bigr)^{(3N_{c}-N_{F}-n_{1})/2{\rm n}_{2}}\ll m_{1}
the hierarchies look as:

{\overline{\mu}}_{\rm gl,1}\gg\Lambda^{\prime\prime}\gg\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,1}
, and
therefore the quarks
q_{1},{\overline{q}}^{\,1}
are higgsed in the weak coupling
region at
\mu={\overline{\mu}}_{\rm gl,1}\gg\Lambda^{\prime\prime}
, the overall
phase is also
Higgs_{1}-Hq_{2}
. The mass spectrum looks in this region
as follows (as previously, all mass values are given below up to logarithmic
factors). -

1) There are
2n_{1}n_{2}
not confined dual quarks
q_{2},{\overline{q}}^{\,2}
with
broken   colors, their masses (up to logarithmic factors) are
\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,2}\sim\langle M_{2}\rangle_{\rm br2}/\Lambda_{Q}\sim m_{Q}\mu_{\Phi}/\Lambda_{Q}
.

2) The quarks
q_{2}^{\prime},{\overline{q}}^{\,\prime,\,2}
with unbroken

SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-n_{1})
dual colors are confined and there is a large number of dual
hadrons made from
these weakly coupled and weakly confined non-relativistic quarks, the scale of
their masses is also
\mu_{H}\sim(m_{Q}\mu_{\Phi}/\Lambda_{Q})
(the tension of the confining
string originated from
SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-n_{1})\,\,\,{\cal N}=1
SYM is

\sqrt{\sigma}\sim\langle{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(\rm br2)}\rangle\ll\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,2}
).

3)
n_{1}(2{\overline{N}}_{c}-n_{1})
massive gluons (and their super-partners) due to higgsing

SU({\overline{N}}_{c})\rightarrow SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-n_{1})
by
q_{1},{\overline{q}}^{\,1}
quarks,
\,\,{\overline{\mu}}^{\,\rm pole}_{\rm gl,1}\sim(m_{Q}\Lambda_{Q})^{1/2}\ll\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,2}
.

3)
n_{1}^{2}
nions
N_{1}^{1}
and
n_{1}^{2}
mions
M_{1}^{1}
also have masses

\mu^{\rm pole}(N_{1}^{1})\sim\mu^{\rm pole}(M_{1}^{1})\sim(m_{Q}\Lambda_{Q})^{1/2}
.

4) There is a large number of gluonia from
SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-n_{1})\,\,\,{\cal N}=1
SYM,
the scale of their masses is

\sim\langle{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(\rm br2)}\rangle\sim(m_{Q}\langle M_{1}\rangle_{\rm br2})^{1/3}\sim\Lambda_{Q}(m_{Q}/\Lambda_{Q})^{({\rm n_{2}-n_{1}})/3({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}\,(\mu_{\Phi}/\Lambda_{Q})^{{\rm n}_{2}/3({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm\,n}_{1})}
.

5)
n_{2}^{2}
mions
M_{2}^{2}
have masses
\mu^{\rm pole}(M_{2}^{2})\sim\Lambda_{Q}^{2}/\mu_{\Phi}
.

6)
2n_{1}n_{2}
mions
M_{1}^{2}
and
M_{2}^{1}
are Nambu-Goldstone particles and
are massless,
\mu(M_{1}^{2})=\mu(M_{2}^{1})=0
.

The overall hierarchy of nonzero masses looks in this range

(\Lambda_{Q}^{3}/m_{Q})^{1/2}<\mu<{\mu}_{\Phi}^{\prime\prime}
as

\displaystyle\mu(M_{2}^{2})\ll\langle{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(\rm br2)}\rangle\ll{\overline{\mu}}_{\rm gl,1}\sim\mu(N_{1}^{1})\sim\mu(M_{1}^{1})\ll\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,2}\ll\Lambda_{Q}\,.

(34.2.4)

But the hierarchies look as
\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,1}\sim(\langle M_{1}\rangle_{\rm br2}/\Lambda_{Q})\gg\Lambda^{\prime\prime}\gg{\overline{\mu}}_{\rm gl,1}
at
\mu_{\Phi}^{\prime\prime}\ll\mu_{\Phi}\ll m_{1}
, the
quarks
q_{1},{\overline{q}}^{\,1}
are then too heavy and not higgsed, and the
overall phase is
Hq_{2}-Hq_{1}
(heavy quarks). The mass spectrum looks
in this region as follows.

1) All quarks are confined and there is a large number of dual hadrons made
from these weakly coupled and weakly confined non-relativistic quarks, the
scale   of their masses is
\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,2}\sim(m_{Q}\mu_{\Phi}/\Lambda_{Q})\gg\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,1}\sim(\langle M_{1}\rangle_{\rm br2}/\Lambda_{Q})=(\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{\rm br2}/\Lambda_{Q})
, see (
34.1.1
), (the tension of the confining string
originatedfrom
SU({\overline{N}}_{c})\,\,\,{\cal N}=1
SYM is

\sqrt{\sigma}\sim\langle{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(\rm br2)}\rangle\ll\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,1}\ll\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,2})
.

2) A large number of gluonia from
SU({\overline{N}}_{c})\,\,\,{\cal N}=1
SYM, the scale of
their masses is

\sim\langle{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(\rm br2)}\rangle\sim(m_{Q}\langle M_{1}\rangle_{\rm br2})^{1/3}\sim\Lambda_{Q}(m_{Q}/\Lambda_{Q})^{({\rm n_{2}-n_{1}})/3({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}(\mu_{\Phi}/\Lambda_{Q})^{{\rm n}_{2}/3({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}
.

3)
n_{1}^{2}
mions
M_{1}^{1}
with masses
\mu^{\rm pole}(M_{1}^{1})\sim\Lambda_{Q}^{2}\langle M_{2}\rangle/\mu_{\Phi}\langle M_{1}\rangle
.

4)
n_{2}^{2}
mions
M_{2}^{2}
with masses
\mu^{\rm pole}(M_{2}^{2})\sim(\Lambda_{Q}^{2}/\mu_{\Phi})\ll\mu^{\rm pole}(M_{1}^{1})
.

5)
2{\rm n}_{1}{\rm n}_{2}
mions
M_{1}^{2}
and
M_{2}^{1}
are the massless
Nambu-Goldstone particles.

The overall hierarchy of nonzero masses look in this range

\mu_{\Phi}^{\prime\prime}<\mu_{\Phi}<m_{1}
as

\displaystyle\mu^{\rm pole}(M_{2}^{2})\ll\mu^{\rm pole}(M_{1}^{1})\ll\langle{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(\rm br2)}\rangle\ll\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,1}\ll\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,2}\ll\Lambda_{Q}\,.

(34.2.5)

Comparing with the direct theory in section 34.1 it is seen that the mass
spectra are parametrically different.

35

Unbroken flavor symmetry,   QCD vacua

35.1

Direct theory.

The direct theory is in the strong coupling regime at
\mu<\Lambda_{Q}
. The quark
condensates look here as, see section 16,

\displaystyle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{\rm QCD}\simeq\Lambda_{Q}^{2}\Bigl(\frac{m_{Q}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{{\overline{N}}_{c}/N_{c}},\,\,\langle{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{\rm(QCD)}\rangle^{3}\equiv\langle S\rangle_{\rm QCD}\simeq\Bigl(\Lambda_{Q}^{\rm\rm{b_{o}}}m_{Q}^{N_{F}}\Bigr)^{1/N_{c}},\,\,\rm{b_{o}}=3N_{c}-N_{F}\,.

(35.1.1)

The potentially important masses look here as

\langle m^{\rm tot}_{Q}\rangle_{\rm QCD}=\langle m_{Q}-\Phi\rangle_{\rm QCD}=\frac{\langle S\rangle_{\rm QCD}}{({\overline{Q}}Q)_{\rm QCD}}\simeq m_{Q}\,,

\displaystyle m^{\rm pole}_{Q,\rm QCD}=\frac{\langle m^{\rm tot}_{Q}\rangle_{\rm QCD}}{z_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q},m^{\rm pole}_{Q,\rm QCD})}\sim\Lambda_{Q}\Bigl(\frac{m_{Q}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{{\overline{N}}_{c}/N_{c}},\,\,z_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q},m^{\rm pole}_{Q,\rm QCD})=\Bigl(\frac{m^{\rm pole}_{Q,\rm QCD}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\gamma_{Q}=(2N_{c}-N_{F})/{\overline{N}}_{c}}\,,

(35.1.2)

\displaystyle\mu^{2}_{\rm gl,QCD}\sim a_{+}(\mu=\mu_{\rm gl,QCD})z_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu_{\rm gl,QCD})({\overline{Q}}Q)_{\rm QCD}\rightarrow\mu_{\rm gl,QCD}\sim\frac{({\overline{Q}}Q)_{\rm QCD}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\sim m^{\rm pole}_{Q,\rm QCD}\,,

(35.1.3)

a_{+}(\mu=\mu_{\rm gl,QCD})\sim\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{\mu_{\rm gl,QCD}}\Bigr)^{\nu_{Q}=(3N_{c}-2N_{F})/{\overline{N}}_{c}},\hskip 11.74988pta_{+}(\mu=\mu_{\rm gl,QCD})\,z_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu_{\rm gl,QCD})\sim\frac{\mu_{\rm gl,QCD}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\,.

As before for vacua with the unbroken flavor symmetry, this implies from the
rank restriction at
N_{F}>N_{c}
that quarks are not higgsed but confined and
the overall phase is HQ (heavy quark).

Besides, see (
34.1.4
) for
\mu_{o}^{\rm str}
and (
35.1.2
),

\displaystyle\frac{m^{\rm pole}_{Q,\rm QCD}}{\mu_{o}^{\rm str}}\sim\Bigl(\frac{\mu_{\Phi}}{{\hat{\mu}}_{\Phi,\rm QCD}}\Bigr)^{\frac{{\overline{N}}_{c}}{5N_{c}-3N_{F}}},\,\,{\hat{\mu}}_{\Phi,\rm QCD}=\Lambda_{Q}\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{m_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\frac{5N_{c}-3N_{F}}{N_{c}}}\gg m_{1},\,\,\frac{1}{2}<\frac{5N_{c}-3N_{F}}{N_{c}}<2.\,\,\,

(35.1.4)

It follows from (
35.1.4
) that :  a)
\,{m^{\rm pole}_{Q,\rm QCD}}<\mu_{o}^{\rm str}

in the range
m_{1}<\mu_{\Phi}<{\hat{\mu}}_{\Phi,\rm QCD}
and so there will be two
additional generations of
\Phi
-particles,
\mu_{2}^{\rm pole}(\Phi)\sim\mu_{o}^{\rm str}

and
\mu_{3}^{\rm pole}(\Phi)\ll m^{\rm pole}_{Q,\rm QCD}
, and all
N_{F}^{2}

fions
\Phi^{j}_{i}
will be dynamically relevant at scales
\mu_{3}^{\rm pole}(\Phi)<\mu<\mu_{2}^{\rm pole}(\Phi)
;  b)  while
{m^{\rm pole}_{Q,\rm QCD}}>\mu_{o}^{\rm str}
at
\mu_{\Phi}>{\hat{\mu}}_{\Phi,\rm QCD}
and all fions will be too heavy
and dynamically irrelevant at all scales
\mu<\mu_{1}^{\rm pole}(\Phi)\sim\mu_{\Phi}
.  Proceeding further in a ”standard” way, i.e.
integrating   out first all quarks   as heavy ones at
\mu<m^{\rm pole}_{]}{Q,\rm QCD}
, the scale factor of the lower energy

SU(N_{c})
SYM in the strong coupling regime is determined from the
matching

a_{+}(\mu=m^{\rm pole}_{Q,\rm QCD})=\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{m^{\rm pole}_{Q,\rm QCD}}\Bigr)^{(3N_{c}-2N_{F})/{\overline{N}}_{c}}=a^{\rm str}_{\rm YM}(\mu=m^{\rm pole}_{Q,\rm QCD})=\Bigl(\frac{m^{\rm pole}_{Q,\rm QCD}}{\langle{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(\rm QCD)}\rangle}\Bigr)^{3}\hskip 11.74988pt\rightarrow

\displaystyle\rightarrow\hskip 11.74988pt\langle{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(\rm QCD)}\rangle=\Bigl(\Lambda_{Q}^{3N_{c}-N_{F}}m_{Q}^{N_{F}}\Bigr)^{1/3N_{c}}

(35.1.5)

as it should be, see (
35.1.1
).

Integrating out now all
SU(N_{c})
gluons at
\mu<{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(\rm QCD)}
via the
VY-procedure
[
15
]
,
we obtain the low energy Lagrangian of
N_{F}^{2}
fions
\Phi^{j}_{i}

\displaystyle K=z_{\Phi}(\Lambda_{Q},m^{\rm pole}_{Q,\rm QCD})\,{\rm Tr}\,(\Phi^{\dagger}\Phi),z_{\Phi}(\Lambda_{Q},m^{\rm pole}_{Q,\rm QCD})=\Bigl(\frac{m^{\rm pole}_{Q}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\gamma_{\Phi}}=\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{m^{\rm pole}_{Q}}\Bigr)^{2(2N_{c}-N_{F})/{\overline{N}}_{c}}\gg 1,\,\,\,\,

(35.1.6)

{\cal W}={\cal W}_{\Phi}+N_{c}\Bigl(\Lambda_{Q}^{\rm\rm{b_{o}}}\det m^{\rm tot}_{Q}\Bigr)^{1/N_{c}}\,,\hskip 11.74988ptm^{\rm tot}_{Q}=m_{Q}-\Phi\,,\hskip 11.74988pt{\cal W}_{\Phi}=\frac{\mu_{\Phi}}{2}\Biggl[{\rm Tr}\,(\Phi^{2})-\frac{1}{{\overline{N}}_{c}}\Bigl({\rm Tr}\,\Phi\Bigr)^{2}\Biggr].

The main contribution to the masses of
N_{F}^{2}
3-rd generation fions at

m_{1}<\mu_{\Phi}<{\hat{\mu}}_{\Phi,\rm QCD}
originates from the term
{\cal W}_{\Phi}

in (
35.1.6
)

\displaystyle\mu_{3}^{\rm pole}(\Phi)\sim\frac{\mu_{\Phi}}{z_{\Phi}(\Lambda_{Q},m^{\rm pole}_{Q,\rm QCD})}\sim\mu_{\Phi}\Bigl(\frac{m_{Q}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{2(2N_{c}-N_{F})/N_{c}}\,,

(35.1.7)

\hskip 11.74988pt\frac{\mu_{3}^{\rm pole}(\Phi)}{m^{\rm pole}_{Q,\rm QCD}}\sim\frac{\mu_{\Phi}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigl(\frac{m_{Q}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\frac{5N_{c}-3N_{F}}{3N_{c}}}=\frac{\mu_{\Phi}}{{\hat{\mu}}_{\Phi,\rm QCD}}<1\,.

On the whole for the mass spectra in these
N_{c}
QCD-vacua at

\mu_{\Phi}>m_{1}=\Lambda_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q}/m_{Q})^{(2N_{c}-N_{F})/N_{c}}
. The overall phase is HQ, all quarks

{\overline{Q}}_{j},Q^{i}
are not higgsed but confined.

A) The range
m_{1}=\Lambda_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q}/m_{Q})^{(2N_{c}-N_{F})/N_{c}}<\mu_{\Phi}<{\hat{\mu}}_{\Phi,\rm QCD}=\Lambda_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q}/m_{Q})^{(5N_{c}-3N_{F})/N_{c}}
.

1) The largest among masses
<\Lambda_{Q}
are masses of
N_{F}^{2}
second
generation fions
\Phi^{j}_{i}
with
\mu_{2}^{\rm pole}(\Phi)\sim\mu_{o}^{\rm str}=\Lambda_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q}/\mu_{\Phi})^{{\overline{N}}_{c}/(5N_{c}-3N_{F})}
.

2) The next are masses
m^{\rm pole}_{Q,\rm QCD}\sim\Lambda_{Q}(m_{Q}/\Lambda_{Q})^{{\overline{N}}_{c}/N_{c}}

of strongly interacting but weakly confined quarks (the tension of the
confiningstring originating from
{\cal N}=1\,\,SU(N_{c})
SYM is

\sqrt{\sigma}\sim{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(\rm QCD)}\ll m^{\rm pole}_{Q,\rm QCD}
).

3) There are
N_{F}^{2}
third generation fions
\Phi^{j}_{i}
with
\mu_{3}^{\rm pole}(\Phi)\sim\mu_{\Phi}(m_{Q}/\Lambda_{Q})^{2(2N_{c}-N_{F})/N_{c}}
.

4) A large number of gluonia from
{\cal N}=1\,\,SU(N_{c})
SYM, the scale of
their masses is
{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(\rm QCD)}\sim(\Lambda_{Q}^{\rm\rm{b_{o}}}m_{Q}^{N_{F}})^{1/3N_{c}}
.

The mass hierarchies look as

\displaystyle\mu_{3}^{\rm pole}(\Phi)\ll m^{\rm pole}_{Q,\rm QCD}\ll\mu_{2}^{\rm pole}(\Phi)=\mu_{o}^{\rm str}\ll\Lambda_{Q}\ll\mu_{1}^{\rm pole}(\Phi)\sim\mu_{\Phi}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(\rm QCD)}\ll m^{\rm pole}_{Q,\rm QCD}\,.

(35.1.8)

B) The range
\mu_{\Phi}>{\hat{\mu}}_{\Phi,\rm QCD}=\Lambda_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q}/m_{Q})^{(5N_{c}-3N_{F})/N_{c}}\gg{\tilde{\mu}}_{\Phi,\rm QCD}\gg m_{1}
.

The difference is that there are no additional generations of all fions in this
case because
m^{\rm pole}_{Q,\rm QCD}>\mu_{o}^{\rm str}
, and all fions remain too heavy
and dynamically irrelevant at all scales
\mu<\mu_{1}^{\rm pole}(\Phi)\sim\mu_{\Phi}
.

35.2

Dual theory.

The dual theory is in the IR free logarithmic regime at scales

\mu_{q,QCD}^{\rm pole}<\mu<\Lambda_{Q}
(as before, all logarithmic factors are
ignored   for simplicity). The potentially important masses look
here as follows. The masses of dual quarks

\displaystyle\mu_{q,QCD}^{\rm pole}\sim\frac{\langle M\rangle_{\rm QCD}}{\Lambda_{Q}}=\frac{\langle{\overline{Q}}Q\rangle_{\rm QCD}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\sim\Lambda_{Q}\Bigl(\frac{m_{Q}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\frac{{\overline{N}}_{c}}{N_{c}}}\sim m^{\rm pole}_{Q,\rm QCD}.

(35.2.1)

The masses of dual gluons due to possible higgsing of dual quarks

\displaystyle{\overline{\mu}}^{\,\rm pole}_{\rm gl,QCD}\sim\langle({\overline{q}}q)\rangle^{1/2}_{\rm QCD}\sim(m_{Q}\Lambda_{Q})^{1/2},\hskip 11.74988pt\frac{{\overline{\mu}}^{\,\rm pole}_{\rm gl,QCD}}{\mu_{q,QCD}^{\rm pole}}\sim\Bigl(\frac{m_{Q}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\frac{3N_{c}-2N_{F}}{2N_{c}}}\ll 1\,.

(35.2.2)

Therefore, the overall phase is Hq (heavy quark), all quarks
{\overline{q}}^{j},q_{i}
are not higgsed but confined. All
N_{F}^{2}
fions
\Phi^{j}_{i}
have large
masses
\mu^{\rm pole}_{1}(\Phi)\sim\mu_{\Phi}\gg\Lambda_{Q}
and are irrelevant at scales

\mu<\mu_{\Phi}
, they can be integrated out from the beginning. After integrating
out all dual quarks as heavy ones at
\mu<\mu_{q,QCD}^{\rm pole}
and then all
dual gluons at
\mu<\langle{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(QCD)}\rangle
via the VY procedure

[
15
]
,   the low energy Lagrangian of
N_{F}^{2}
mions
M^{i}_{j}
looks as

\displaystyle K\sim\,{\rm Tr}\,\frac{M^{\dagger}M}{\Lambda_{Q}^{2}},\,\,{\cal W}={\cal W}_{M}-{\overline{N}}_{c}\Bigl(\frac{\det M}{\Lambda_{Q}^{\rm\rm{b_{o}}}}\Bigr)^{1/{\overline{N}}_{c}},\,\,{\cal W}_{M}=m_{Q}{\rm Tr}\,M-\frac{1}{2\mu_{\Phi}}\Bigl[{\rm Tr}\,(M^{2})-\frac{1}{N_{c}}\Bigl({\rm Tr}\,M\Bigr)^{2}\Bigr].\,\,\,\,

(35.2.3)

The main contribution to the masses of
N_{F}^{2}
mions originates from the
nonperturbative term in (
35.2.3
)

\displaystyle\mu^{\rm pole}(M)\sim\frac{\langle S\rangle_{\rm QCD}\Lambda_{Q}^{2}}{\langle M\rangle^{2}_{\rm QCD}}\sim\Lambda_{Q}\Bigl(\frac{m_{Q}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{(2N_{c}-N_{F})/N_{c}}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\frac{\mu^{\rm pole}(M)}{{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(QCD)}}\sim\Bigl(\frac{m_{Q}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\frac{2(3N_{c}-2N_{F})}{3N_{c}}}\ll 1\,.

(35.2.4)

Comparing with the direct theory in section 35.1 it is seen that at

m_{1}<\mu_{\Phi}<{\hat{\mu}}_{\Phi,\rm QCD}
the masses
\mu_{3}^{\rm pole}(\Phi)

(
35.1.7
) and
\mu^{\rm pole}(M)
(
35.2.4
) are parametrically
different (the triangles
SU^{3}(N_{F})_{L}
are also different). Besides, there are
no particles with masses smaller than
\langle{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(QCD)}\rangle
at

\mu_{\Phi}>{\hat{\mu}}_{\Phi,\rm QCD}
in the direct theory while
\mu^{\rm pole}(M)\ll\langle{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(QCD)}\rangle
in the dual one, see (
35.2.4
).

36

Broken flavor symmetry.  Direct theory.

36.1

br1 and special vacua with
1\leq n_{1}<{\rm Min}\,(N_{F}/2,\,3N_{c}-2N_{F})

The condensates look in this case as, see section 16,

\displaystyle\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{\rm br1}\simeq\frac{N_{c}}{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}m_{Q}\mu_{\Phi}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{\rm br1}\sim\Bigl(\frac{m_{Q}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\frac{{\rm n}_{2}-N_{c}}{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}}\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\Bigr)^{\frac{{\rm n}_{1}}{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}}\,,

(36.1.1)

\langle{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{\rm(br1)}\rangle^{3}\equiv\langle S\rangle_{\rm br1}=\frac{\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{\rm br1}\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{\rm br1}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\sim\Bigl(\frac{m_{Q}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\frac{{\rm n}_{2}-{\rm n}_{1}}{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}}\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\Bigr)_{,}^{\frac{{\rm n}_{1}}{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}}\hskip 11.74988pt\frac{\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{\rm br2}}{\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{\rm br2}}\sim\Bigl(\frac{m_{1}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\Bigr)^{\frac{N_{c}}{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}}\ll 1\,.

The potentially important masses look as :

a) the quark masses,

\displaystyle\langle m_{Q,1}^{\rm tot}\rangle\ll\langle m_{Q,2}^{\rm tot}\rangle=\langle m_{Q}-\Phi_{2}\rangle=\frac{\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle}{\mu_{\Phi}}\sim m_{Q},\hskip 11.74988pt{\tilde{m}}^{\rm pole}_{Q,2}=\frac{\langle m_{Q,2}^{\rm tot}\rangle}{z_{Q}^{+}(\Lambda_{Q},\langle m_{Q,2}^{\rm tot}\rangle)}\sim\Lambda_{Q}\Bigl(\frac{m_{Q}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{{\overline{N}}_{c}/N_{c}},

(36.1.2)

b) the gluon masses due to possible higgsing of quarks,

(\mu_{\rm gl,1})^{2}\sim a_{+}(\mu_{\rm gl,1})\,z_{Q}^{+}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu_{\rm gl,1})\,\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle,\hskip 11.74988pta_{+}(\mu_{\rm gl,1})=\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{\mu_{\rm gl,1}}\Bigr)^{\nu_{+}},\,\,z_{Q}^{+}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu_{\rm gl,1})=\Bigl(\frac{\mu_{\rm gl,1}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\gamma^{+}_{Q}},

\displaystyle\mu_{\rm gl,1}\sim\frac{\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle}{\Lambda_{Q}}\sim\frac{m_{Q}\mu_{\Phi}}{\Lambda_{Q}},\hskip 11.74988pt\,\frac{{\tilde{m}}^{\rm pole}_{Q,2}}{\mu_{\rm gl,1}}\sim\frac{m_{1}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\ll 1,\hskip 11.74988pt\hskip 11.74988pt\gamma^{+}_{Q}=\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{N_{F}-N_{c}},\hskip 11.74988pt\nu^{+}=\frac{3N_{c}-2N_{F}}{N_{F}-N_{c}}\,.

(36.1.3)

Therefore, the quarks
Q^{1},{\overline{Q}}_{1}
are higgsed and the overall phase
is
Higgs_{1}-HQ_{2}
in this case. Besides,

\displaystyle\frac{\mu_{\rm gl,1}}{\mu_{o}^{\rm str}}\sim\Bigl(\frac{\mu_{\Phi}}{\mu_{\Phi,1}}\Bigr)^{\frac{2(2N_{c}-N_{F})}{5N_{c}-3N_{F}}}>1\,\,\,{\rm only\,at}\,\,\,\mu_{\Phi}>\mu_{\Phi,1}=\Lambda_{Q}\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{m_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\frac{5N_{c}-3N_{F}}{2(2N_{c}-N_{F})}},

(36.1.4)

\mu_{o}^{\rm str}=\Lambda_{Q}\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\Bigr)^{\frac{1}{2\gamma_{Q}^{+}-1}}=\Lambda_{Q}\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\Bigl)^{\frac{{\overline{N}}_{c}}{5N_{c}-3N_{F}}}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\frac{m_{1}}{\mu_{\Phi,1}}\sim\Bigl(\frac{m_{Q}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\frac{{\overline{N}}_{c}(3N_{c}-2N_{F})}{2N_{c}(2N_{c}-N_{F})}}\ll 1,\hskip 11.74988ptm_{1}=\Lambda_{Q}\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{m_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{N_{c}}}\,,

this shows that the fions
\Phi^{1}_{1},\Phi_{1}^{2}
and
\Phi_{2}^{1}
become too heavy
and dynamically irrelevant at all scales
\mu<\mu_{1}^{\rm pole}(\Phi)\sim\mu_{\Phi}\gg\Lambda_{Q}
when
\mu_{\Phi}>\mu_{\Phi,1}
only (the situation with
\Phi^{2}_{2}
is
different, see below), while at
m_{1}<\mu_{\Phi}<\mu_{\Phi,1}
all
N_{F}^{2}
fions
become relevant at the scale
\mu<\mu^{\rm pole}_{2}(\Phi)=\mu_{o}^{\rm str}=\Lambda_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q}/\mu_{\Phi})^{{\overline{N}}_{c}/(5N_{c}-3N_{F})}
and there appears
additional second generation of all
N_{F}^{2}\,\,\Phi
-particles.

A)    The region
m_{1}\ll\mu_{\Phi}\ll\mu_{\Phi,1}=\Lambda_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q}/m_{Q})^{\frac{5N_{c}-3N_{F}}{2(2N_{c}-N_{F})}}
.

Because quarks
{\overline{Q}}_{1},Q^{1}
are higgsed at
\mu\sim\mu_{\rm gl,1}
,
the lower energy theory at
\mu<\mu_{\rm gl,1}
has the unbroken gauge
group
SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})
,
n_{2}
flavors of quarks
{\overline{Q}}^{\,\prime}_{2},Q^{\,\prime,\,2}
with unbroken colors,
N_{F}^{2}
fions
\Phi^{j}_{i}
,
\,{\rm n}_{1}^{2}

pions
\Pi^{1}_{1}
(these originated from higgsing of
{\overline{Q}}_{1},Q^{1}
), and
finally
2{\rm n}_{1}{\rm n}_{2}
hybrid pions
\Pi^{1}_{2},\,\Pi^{2}_{1}
(these in essence are
quarks
{\overline{Q}}_{2},Q^{2}
with broken colors).

In case
1\leq{\rm n}_{1}<{\rm Min}(N_{F}/2,\,3N_{c}-2N_{F})
, i.e. either any
1\leq{\rm n}_{1}<N_{F}/2
at
N_{c}<N_{F}<6N_{c}/5
, or
{\rm n}_{1}<(3N_{c}-2N_{F})
at
6N_{c}/5<N_{F}<3N_{c}/2
,
the lower energy theory at
\mu<\mu_{\rm gl,1}
remains in the strong coupling region
with:
N_{c}^{\prime}=(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}),\,\,N_{F}^{\prime}={\rm n}_{2},\,\,1<N_{F}^{\prime}/N_{c}^{\prime}<3/2\,,\,\,{\rm b}^{\prime}_{o}=(3N_{c}^{\prime}-N_{F}^{\prime})=(3N_{c}-N_{F}-2{\rm n}_{1})>0
.

The anomalous dimensions and the gauge coupling look in this lower energy
theory as, see Appendix A for the values of anomalous dimensions in
the strong coupling region,

\displaystyle\gamma_{Q}^{-}=\frac{2N_{c}^{\prime}-N_{F}^{\prime}}{N_{F}^{\prime}-N_{c}^{\prime}}=\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}-{\rm n}_{1}}{(N_{F}-N_{c})={\overline{N}}_{c}}=\gamma_{Q}^{+}-\frac{{\rm n}_{1}}{{\overline{N}}_{c}}\,>\,1\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\gamma_{\Phi}^{\pm}=-2\gamma_{Q}^{\pm}\,,

(36.1.5)

a_{-}(\mu<\mu_{\rm gl,1})=\Bigl[a_{+}(\mu=\mu_{\rm gl,1})=\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{\mu_{\rm gl,1}}\Bigr)^{\nu_{+}}\Bigr]\Bigl(\frac{\mu_{\rm gl,1}}{\mu}\Bigr)^{\nu_{-}},\hskip 11.74988pt\nu_{+}=\frac{3N_{c}-2N_{F}}{{\overline{N}}_{c}},\hskip 11.74988pt\nu_{-}=\nu_{+}-\frac{{\rm n}_{1}}{{\overline{N}}_{c}}>0\,.

The pole mass of
{\overline{Q}}^{\,\prime}_{2},Q^{\,\prime,\,2}
quarks and
\mu_{\rm gl,2}
due
to possible higgsing of these quarks look then as, see
(
36.1.1
),(
36.1.3
),

m_{Q,2}^{\rm pole}=\frac{\langle m_{Q,2}^{\rm tot}\rangle}{z^{+}_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu_{\rm gl,1})\,z^{-}_{Q}(\mu_{\rm gl,1},m_{Q,2}^{\rm pole})}\,,\,\,z^{+}_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu_{\rm gl,1})=\Bigl(\frac{\mu_{\rm gl,1}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\gamma^{+}_{Q}},\,\,z^{-}_{Q}(\mu_{\rm gl,1},m_{Q,2}^{\rm pole})=\Bigl(\frac{m_{Q,2}^{\rm pole}}{\mu_{\rm gl,1}}\Bigr)^{\gamma^{-}_{Q}}\,,

\displaystyle m_{Q,2}^{\rm pole}\sim\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\Bigr)^{\frac{{\rm n}_{1}}{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}}\,\Bigl(\frac{m_{Q}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\frac{{\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}}\sim\frac{\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle}{\Lambda_{Q}}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\hskip 11.74988pt\frac{m_{Q,2}^{\rm pole}}{\mu_{\rm gl,1}}\sim\Bigl(\frac{m_{1}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\Bigr)^{\frac{N_{c}}{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}}\ll 1\,,

(36.1.6)

(\mu_{\rm gl,2})^{2}\sim\rho_{+}\rho_{-}\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle,\,\,\rho_{+}=a_{+}(\mu_{\rm gl,1})\,z_{Q}^{+}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu_{\rm gl,1})\sim\frac{\mu_{\rm gl,1}}{\Lambda_{Q}},\,\,\rho_{-}\sim\Bigl(\frac{\mu_{\rm gl,1}}{\mu_{\rm gl,2}}\Bigr)^{\nu^{-}}z_{Q}^{-}(\mu_{\rm gl,1},\mu_{\rm gl,2})=\frac{\mu_{\rm gl,2}}{\mu_{\rm gl,1}}\,,

\rho_{+}\rho_{-}\sim\frac{\mu_{\rm gl,2}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\mu_{\rm gl,2}\sim m_{Q,2}^{\rm pole}\sim\frac{\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle}{\Lambda_{Q}}\sim\Bigl(\frac{m_{Q}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\frac{{\rm n}_{2}-N_{c}}{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}}\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\Bigr)^{\frac{{\rm n}_{1}}{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}}\,.

Because
{\rm n}_{2}-(N_{c}^{\prime}=N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})=N_{F}-N_{c}>0
, the rank restriction shows that
the qurks
{\overline{Q}}^{\,\prime}_{2},Q^{\,\prime,\,2}
are not higgsed because
otherwise the global
U({\rm n}_{2})
flavor symmetry will be
additionally
broken
spontaneously (and this will be wrong).

Therefore, after integrating out remained active quarks
{\overline{Q}}^{\,\prime}_{2},Q^{\,\prime,\,2}
with unbroken
N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}
colors as heavy ones at

\mu<m_{Q,2}^{\rm pole}
, the scale factor of the lower energy
SU(N_{c})
SYM in
the strong coupling regime is determined from the matching, see
(
36.1.3
),(
36.1.5
),(
36.1.6
),

\displaystyle a_{-}(\mu=m_{Q,2}^{\rm pole})=\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{\mu_{\rm gl,1}}\Bigr)^{\nu_{+}}\,\Bigl(\frac{\mu_{\rm gl,1}}{m_{Q,2}^{\rm pole}}\Bigr)^{\nu_{-}}=a_{\rm YM}^{(\rm br1)}(\mu=m_{Q,2}^{\rm pole})=\Bigl(\frac{m_{Q,2}^{\rm pole}}{\lambda_{YM}}\Bigr)^{3}\gg 1\,,

(36.1.7)

and is

\displaystyle\lambda_{YM}^{3}=\Bigl(\langle{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(\rm br1)}\rangle\Bigr)^{3}=\Lambda_{Q}^{3}\Bigl(\frac{m_{Q}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\frac{{\rm n}_{2}-{\rm n}_{1}}{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}}\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\Bigr)^{\frac{{\rm n}_{1}}{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}}=\langle S\rangle_{\rm br1}=\frac{\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{\rm br1}\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{\rm br1}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\,,

(36.1.8)

as it should be, while

\displaystyle\frac{\langle{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(\rm br1)}\rangle}{m_{Q,2}^{\rm pole}}=\Bigl(\frac{m_{Q}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\frac{3N_{c}-2N_{F}+{\rm n}_{1}}{3(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}}\Bigl(\frac{\mu_{\Phi}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\frac{2{\rm n}_{1}}{3(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}}=\Bigl(\frac{\mu_{\Phi}}{{\mu}_{\Phi,3}}\Bigr)^{\frac{2{\rm n}_{1}}{3(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}}\ll 1

(36.1.9)

{\rm at}\,\hskip 11.74988pt\mu_{\Phi}\ll{\rm Min}\,\Bigl\{\frac{\Lambda_{Q}^{2}}{m_{Q}},\,{\mu}_{\Phi,3}\Bigr\},\hskip 11.74988pt{\mu}_{\Phi,3}=\Lambda_{Q}(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{m_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\frac{3N_{c}-2N_{F}+{\rm n}_{1}}{2{\rm n}_{1}}}\,,

(at
\mu_{\Phi}>\Lambda_{Q}^{2}/m_{Q}
the quarks are higgsed in the weak coupling
regime, see (
36.1.1
)).

Integrating out finally all
SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})
gluons at
\mu<\langle{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(\rm br1)}\rangle\ll m_{Q,2}^{\rm pole}
via the VY-procedure
[
15
]
, the lower
energy Lagrangian of
N_{F}^{2}
3-rd generation fions
\Phi_{i}^{j}
,
{\rm n}_{1}^{2}
pions

\Pi^{i}_{j}
and
2{\rm n}_{1}{\rm n}_{2}
hybrid pions
\Pi^{1}_{2},\,\Pi^{2}_{1}
(in essence, these
are
{\overline{Q}}_{2},Q^{2}
quarks with broken colors) look as, see
[
26
]
,

\displaystyle K_{\rm tot}=K_{\Phi}+K_{\Pi}+K^{\rm hybr}_{\Pi}\,,

(36.1.10)

K_{\Phi}=z_{\Phi}^{+}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu_{\rm gl,1})\,{\rm Tr}\,\Bigl[(\Phi_{1}^{1})^{\dagger}\Phi_{1}^{1}+\Bigl(\Phi_{1}^{2})^{\dagger}\Phi_{1}^{2}+(\Phi_{2}^{1})^{\dagger}\Phi_{2}^{1}\Bigr)+z_{\Phi}^{-}(\mu_{\rm gl,1},m_{Q,2}^{\rm pole})\,(\Phi_{2}^{2})^{\dagger}\Phi_{2}^{2}\Bigr]\,,

K_{\Pi}\simeq K^{\rm(loop)}_{\Pi}=\,{\rm Tr}\,\frac{(\Pi_{1}^{1})^{\dagger}\Pi_{1}^{1}}{\Lambda_{Q}^{2}}\,,\hskip 11.74988ptK^{\rm hybr}_{\Pi}=z_{Q}^{+}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu_{\rm gl,1})\,{\rm Tr}\,\Bigl(\,\,\frac{(\Pi_{1}^{2})^{\dagger}\Pi_{1}^{2}+(\Pi_{2}^{1})^{\dagger}\Pi_{2}^{1}}{|\langle\Pi_{1}\rangle|=|\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle|}\,\,\Bigr)\,,

\displaystyle{\cal W}_{\rm tot}={\cal W}_{\Phi}+{\cal W}_{\Phi\Pi}+{\cal W}_{\rm non-pert}\,,

(36.1.11)

{\cal W}_{\Phi}=\frac{\mu_{\Phi}}{2}\Bigl[\,{\rm Tr}\,(\Phi^{2})-\frac{1}{{\overline{N}}_{c}}\,\Bigl({\rm Tr}\,\Phi\Bigr)^{2}\Bigr]\,,\hskip 11.74988pt{\cal W}_{\rm non-pert}=({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}^{\rm\rm{b_{o}}}\det m_{Q,2}^{\rm tot}}{\det\Pi_{1}^{1}}\Bigr)^{\frac{1}{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}}\,,

{\cal W}_{\Phi\Pi}=m_{Q}\,{\rm Tr}\,\Bigl[\Pi_{1}^{1}+\Pi^{2}_{1}\frac{1}{\Pi_{1}^{1}}\Pi^{1}_{2}\Bigr]-\,{\rm Tr}\,\Bigl[\,\Pi_{1}^{1}\Phi_{1}^{1}+\Pi_{1}^{2}\Phi_{2}^{1}+\Pi_{2}^{1}\Phi_{1}^{2}+\Phi_{2}^{2}\,(\Pi^{2}_{1}\frac{1}{\Pi_{1}^{1}}\Pi^{1}_{2})\,\Bigr]\,.

We obtain from (
36.1.10
),(
36.1.11
) for the particle masses. -

The masses of the 3-rd generation fions
\mu^{\rm pole}_{3}(\Phi_{1}^{1})\sim\mu^{\rm pole}_{3}(\Phi_{1}^{2})\sim\mu^{\rm pole}_{3}(\Phi_{2}^{1})
look as

\displaystyle\mu^{\rm pole}_{3}(\Phi_{1}^{1})=\frac{\mu_{\Phi}}{z_{\Phi}^{+}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu_{\rm gl,1})}\sim\Lambda_{Q}\Bigl(\frac{m_{Q}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{2(2N_{c}-N_{F})/{\overline{N}}_{c}}\Bigl(\frac{\mu_{\Phi}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{(3N_{c}-N_{F})/{\overline{N}}_{c}}\,,

(36.1.12)

\frac{\mu^{\rm pole}_{3}(\Phi_{1}^{1})}{\mu_{\rm gl,1}}\sim\Bigl(\frac{\mu_{\Phi}}{\mu_{\Phi,1}}\Bigr)^{2(2N_{c}-N_{F})/{\overline{N}}_{c}}\ll 1\,,

while for
\mu^{\rm pole}_{3}(\Phi_{2}^{2})
,

\displaystyle\mu^{\rm pole}_{3}(\Phi_{2}^{2})\sim\frac{\mu_{\Phi}}{z_{\Phi}^{+}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu_{\rm gl,1})z_{\Phi}^{-}(\mu_{\rm gl,1},m_{Q,2}^{\rm pole})}\sim\Lambda_{Q}\Bigl(\frac{m_{Q}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\frac{2(2N_{c}-N_{F})}{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}}\,\Bigl(\frac{\mu_{\Phi}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\frac{N_{c}+{\rm n}_{1}}{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}}\,,

(36.1.13)

\frac{\mu^{\rm pole}_{3}(\Phi_{2}^{2})}{\mu^{\rm pole}_{3}(\Phi_{1}^{1})}=\frac{1}{z_{\Phi}^{-}(\mu_{\rm gl,1},m_{Q,2}^{\rm pole})}\sim\Bigl(\frac{m_{Q,2}^{\rm pole})}{\mu_{\rm gl,1}}\Bigr)^{2\gamma^{-}_{Q}}\sim\Bigl(\frac{m_{1}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\Bigr)^{\frac{2N_{c}}{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}(\gamma^{-}_{Q}\,>\,1)}\ll 1\,,

\frac{\mu^{\rm pole}_{3}(\Phi_{2}^{2})}{m_{Q,2}^{\rm pole}}\sim\Bigl(\frac{\mu_{\Phi}}{\mu_{\Phi,2}}\Bigr)^{\frac{N_{c}+2{\rm n}_{1}}{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}}\ll 1\hskip 11.74988pt{\rm at}\hskip 11.74988pt\mu_{\Phi}\ll\mu_{\Phi,2},\hskip 11.74988pt\mu_{\Phi,1}\ll\mu_{\Phi,2}=\Lambda_{Q}\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{m_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\frac{5N_{c}-3N_{F}+{\rm n}_{1}}{N_{c}+2{\rm n}_{1}}}

(the main contribution to
\mu^{\rm pole}_{3}(\Phi_{2}^{2})
in (
36.1.13
)
originates from the term
\sim\mu_{\Phi}{\rm Tr}\,(\Phi_{2}^{2})^{2}
in
(
36.1.11
),   while the contribution from
{\cal W}_{\rm non-pert}
is
much smaller ).

The smallest nonzero masses have
{\rm n}_{1}^{2}
pions
\Pi^{1}_{1}
,

\displaystyle\mu^{\rm pole}(\Pi_{1}^{1})\sim\frac{\Lambda_{Q}^{2}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\hskip 11.74988pt\frac{\mu^{\rm pole}(\Pi_{1}^{1})}{\mu^{\rm pole}_{3}(\Phi_{2}^{2})}\sim\Bigl(\frac{m_{1}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\Bigr)^{\frac{2N_{c}}{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}}\ll 1

(36.1.14)

(the main contribution to
\mu^{\rm pole}(\Pi_{1}^{1})
in (
36.1.14
)
originates from the mixing term
\sim{\rm Tr}\,(\Pi_{1}^{1}\Phi_{1}^{1})
in
(
36.1.11
), after integrating out heavier
\Phi^{1}_{1}
, while the
contribution
to
\mu^{\rm pole}(\Pi_{1}^{1})
from
{\cal W}_{\rm non-pert}
is much smaller ).

Finally,
2{\rm n}_{1}{\rm n}_{2}
multiplets
\Pi_{1}^{2}
and
\Pi_{2}^{1}
are Nambu-Goldstone
particles and are massless.

B)   The region
\mu_{\Phi,1}=\Lambda_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q}/m_{Q})^{\frac{5N_{c}-3N_{F}}{2(2N_{c}-N_{F})}}\ll\mu_{\Phi}\ll\Lambda_{Q}^{2}/m_{Q}
.

Because
\mu_{\rm gl,1}>\mu_{o}^{\rm str}=\Lambda_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q}/\mu_{\Phi})^{{\overline{N}}_{c}/(5N_{c}-3N_{F})}
in this case, the running
mass
\mu_{\Phi}(\mu)
of all
N_{F}^{2}
fions
\Phi^{j}_{i}
remains too large at the
scale
\mu=\mu_{\rm gl,1},\,\,\mu_{\Phi}(\mu=\mu_{\rm gl,1})>\mu_{\rm gl,1}
, so that they are still
irrelevant at this scale. Because all heavy higgsed quarks
{\overline{Q}}_{1},Q^{1}

decouple at
\mu<\mu_{\rm gl,1}
, the RG evolution of fions
\Phi_{1}^{1},\Phi_{1}^{2}
and

\Phi_{2}^{1}
is frozen at scales
\mu<\mu_{\rm gl,1}
, so that they remain irrelevant at
all   scales
\mu<\mu_{1}^{\rm pole)}(\Phi)\sim\mu_{\Phi}\gg\Lambda_{Q}
, there are no
additional generations of these particles.

But as for
\Phi_{2}^{2}
, the situation is different because the quarks
{\overline{Q}}^{\,\prime}_{2},Q^{\,\prime,\,2}
with unbroken colors are still active at

\mu<\mu_{\rm gl,1}
and the running mass of fions
\Phi_{2}^{2}
continues to decrease with
decreasing scale (until
{\overline{Q}}^{\,\prime}_{2},Q^{\,\prime,\,2}
decouple at

\mu<m_{Q,2}^{\rm pole}
). As a result, there still will be two additional
generations of
n_{2}^{2}\,\,\Phi_{2}^{2}
-particles in the range

\mu_{\Phi,1}<\mu_{\Phi}<\mu_{\Phi,2}<\Lambda_{Q}^{2}/m_{Q}
, see (
36.1.13
),

\displaystyle\mu^{\rm pole}_{2}(\Phi_{2}^{2})={\hat{\mu}}_{o}^{\,\rm str}=\Lambda_{Q}\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{{\hat{\mu}}_{\Phi}}\Bigr)^{\frac{1}{2\gamma^{-}_{Q}-1}}=\Lambda_{Q}\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{m_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\frac{2n_{1}}{5N_{c}-3N_{F}-2{\rm n}_{1}}}\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\Bigr)^{\frac{{\overline{N}}_{c}+2{\rm n}_{1}}{5N_{c}-3N_{F}-2{\rm n}_{1}}}\,,

(36.1.15)

{\hat{\mu}}_{\Phi}=\mu_{\Phi}\Bigl(\frac{\mu_{\rm gl,1}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{2(\gamma_{Q}^{+}-\gamma_{Q}^{-})}=\Lambda_{Q}\Bigl(\frac{m_{Q}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\frac{2{\rm n}_{1}}{{\overline{N}}_{c}}}\Bigl(\frac{\mu_{\Phi}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\frac{{\overline{N}}_{c}+2{\rm n}_{1}}{{\overline{N}}_{c}}},\hskip 11.74988pt\frac{\mu^{\rm pole}_{2}(\Phi_{2}^{2})}{\mu_{\rm gl,1}}=\Bigl(\frac{\mu_{\Phi,1}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\Bigr)^{\frac{2(2N_{c}-N_{F})}{5N_{c}-3N_{F}-2{\rm n}_{1}}\,>\,0}\ll 1\,.

\displaystyle\frac{m_{Q,2}^{\rm pole}}{\mu^{\rm pole}_{2}(\Phi_{2}^{2})}\sim\Bigl(\frac{\mu_{\Phi}}{\mu_{\Phi,2}}\Bigr)^{\frac{{\overline{N}}_{c}(N_{c}+2{\rm n}_{1})}{(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})(5N_{c}-3N_{F}-2{\rm n}_{1})}>\,0},\,\,\frac{\mu_{\Phi,1}}{\mu_{\Phi,2}}\sim\Bigl(\frac{m_{Q}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\frac{(3N_{c}-2N_{F})(5N_{c}-3N_{F}-2{\rm n}_{1})}{2(2N_{c}-N_{F})(N_{c}+2{\rm n}_{1})}\,>\,0}\ll 1\,.

(36.1.16)

As it is seen from (
36.1.16
), all
{\rm n}_{2}^{2}
fions
\Phi_{2}^{2}
remain
dynamically irrelevant only at
\mu_{\Phi}>\mu_{\Phi,2}\gg\mu_{\Phi,1}\gg m_{1}
,
because the quarks
{\overline{Q}}^{\,\prime}_{2},Q^{\,\prime,\,2}
with unbroken colors
decouple in this case before there appears the second generation of

\Phi_{2}^{2}
, while in the range
\mu_{\Phi,1}\ll\mu_{\Phi}\ll\mu_{\Phi,2}
there
appear two additional generations of
{\rm n}_{2}^{2}\,\,\Phi_{2}^{2}
-particles, and fions

\Phi_{2}^{2}
become dynamically relevant in the interval of scales
\mu^{\rm pole}_{3}(\Phi_{2}^{2})<\mu<\mu^{\rm pole}_{2}(\Phi_{2}^{2})
, see
(
36.1.13
),(
36.1.15
).

But to keep
\mu_{\Phi,2}<\Lambda_{Q}^{2}/m_{Q}
(the RG flow becomes different at

\mu_{\Phi}>\Lambda_{Q}^{2}/m_{Q}
because
\mu_{\rm gl,1}\sim(m_{Q}\mu_{\Phi})/\Lambda_{Q}
becomes larger than
\Lambda_{Q}

and the quarks
{\overline{Q}}_{1},Q^{1}
will be higgsed in the weak coupling regime) it
should be:
8N_{c}/7<N_{F}<3N_{c}/2
. Therefore, at
N_{c}<N_{F}<8N_{c}/7
the
{\rm n}_{2}^{2}

fions
\Phi_{2}^{2}
are relevant in the range of scales
\mu^{\rm pole}_{3}(\Phi_{2}^{2})<\mu<\mu^{\rm pole}_{2}(\Phi_{2}^{2})
in the whole interval
m_{1}<\mu_{\Phi}<\Lambda_{Q}^{2}/m_{Q}
and
there
are two additional generations,
\mu^{\rm pole}_{2}(\Phi_{2}^{2})
and
\mu^{\rm pole}_{3}(\Phi_{2}^{2})
, see e.g. (
36.1.4
). But at

8N_{c}/7<N_{F}<3N_{c}/2
these two additional generations of particles
\Phi_{2}^{2}

exist only in the range
\mu_{\Phi,1}<\mu_{\Phi}<\mu_{\Phi,2}
and disappear at

\mu_{\Phi,2}<\mu_{\Phi}<\Lambda_{Q}^{2}/m_{Q}
, so that there are no additional generations of
any
\Phi
-particles at all at
\mu_{\Phi}>\mu_{\Phi,2}
.

All formulas in special vacua with
{\rm n}_{1}={\overline{N}}_{c},\,{\rm n}_{2}=N_{c}
can be obtained simply
substituting
{\rm n}_{1}={\overline{N}}_{c}
in all expressions in this section 36.1.

36.2

br1 and special vacua with
3N_{c}-2N_{F}<n_{1}<N_{F}/2

Consider now the case
{\rm n}_{1}>3N_{c}-2N_{F}
, this requires
6N_{c}/5<N_{F}<3N_{c}/2

since
{\rm n}_{1}<N_{F}/2
(recall also that we ignore for simplicity all RG evolution
effects if they are logarithmic only). In this case
3/2<N_{F}^{\prime}/N_{c}^{\prime}<3\,,\,\,\,N_{F}^{\prime}=N_{F}-{\rm n}_{1}={\rm n}_{2}\,,\,\,\,N_{c}^{\prime}=N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}
. This means that, after the quarks
{\overline{Q}}_{1},Q^{1}
are higgsed at

\mu\sim\mu_{\rm gl,1}\sim m_{Q}\mu_{\Phi}/\Lambda_{Q}\ll\Lambda_{Q}
in the strong coupling regime

a_{+}(\mu=\mu_{\rm gl,1})\gg 1
, the theory remains with the same large coupling

a_{-}(\mu=\mu_{\rm gl,1})=a_{+}(\mu=\mu_{\rm gl,1})
, but the numbers of colors

N_{c}^{\prime}=N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}
and flavors
N_{F}^{\prime}=N_{F}-{\rm n}_{1}={\rm n}_{2}
in the lower energy
theory are now such that the coupling
a_{-}(\mu)
begins
to decrease

with diminishing scale because the
\beta
-function becomes
positive
at

\mu<\mu_{\rm gl,1}
(until the gauge coupling is large,
a_{-}(\mu)\gg 1
), while the
quark anomalous dimension looks now as

\gamma^{-}_{Q}(N^{\prime}_{c},N^{\prime}_{F},a(\mu)\gg 1)=(2N^{\prime}_{c}-N^{\prime}_{F})/(N^{\prime}_{F}-N^{\prime}_{c})
,

\displaystyle\frac{da_{-}(\mu)}{d\ln\mu}=\frac{a^{2}_{-}(\mu)[\,3-\frac{N_{F}^{\prime}}{N_{c}^{\prime}}(1+\gamma^{-}_{Q})]}{a_{-}(\mu)-1}\simeq a_{-}(\mu)\Bigl[\frac{\,{\rm n}_{1}-(3N_{c}-2N_{F})}{{\overline{N}}_{c}}\Bigr]>0,

(36.2.1)

0\,<\,\gamma^{-}_{Q}=\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}-{\rm n}_{1}}{{\overline{N}}_{c}}\,<\,1,\,\,\gamma^{-}_{\Phi}=-2\gamma^{-}_{Q}\,\,\,{\rm at}\,\,\,(3N_{c}-2N_{F})<{\rm n}_{1}<(2N_{c}-N_{F})\,,\,\,\frac{6N_{c}}{5}<N_{F}<\frac{4N_{c}}{3}\,,

-\,\frac{1}{2}<\,\gamma^{-}_{Q}=\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}-{\rm n}_{1}}{{\overline{N}}_{c}}\,<\,0,\,\,\gamma^{-}_{\Phi}=0\,\,\,{\rm at}\,\,\,(2N_{c}-N_{F})<{\rm n}_{1}<\frac{N_{F}}{2}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\frac{4N_{c}}{3}<N_{F}<\frac{3N_{c}}{2}\,.

The scale factor
\Lambda^{\prime}_{Q}
of the gauge coupling
a_{-}(\mu)
is
determined from the matching

a_{+}(\mu=\mu_{\rm gl,1})=\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{\mu_{\rm gl,1}}\Bigr)^{\frac{3N_{c}-2N_{F}}{N_{F}-N_{c}}}=a_{-}(\mu=\mu_{\rm gl,1})=\Bigl(\frac{\mu_{\rm gl,1}}{\Lambda^{\prime}_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\frac{{\rm n}_{1}-(3N_{c}-2N_{F})}{N_{F}-N_{c}}}\gg 1\,,

\displaystyle\Lambda^{\prime}_{Q}=\Lambda_{Q}\Bigl(\frac{m_{Q}\mu_{\Phi}}{\Lambda_{Q}^{2}}\Bigr)^{\frac{{\rm n}_{1}}{{\rm n}_{1}-(3N_{c}-2N_{F})}\,>\,1}\ll\mu_{\rm gl,1}\sim\frac{m_{Q}\mu_{\Phi}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\ll\Lambda_{Q}\,.

(36.2.2)

Therefore (if nothing prevents, see below), the theory will finally enter
smoothly at the scale
\mu<\Lambda^{\prime}_{Q}
into the conformal regime with the
frozen gauge coupling
a_{*}\sim 1
. But, if
m_{Q,2}^{\rm pole}\gg\Lambda^{\prime}_{Q}
, see (
36.1.9
), the quarks
{\overline{Q}}^{\,\prime}_{2},Q^{\,\prime,\,2}
with unbroken colors will decouple still in the strong
coupling regime
a_{-}(\mu=m_{Q,2}^{\rm pole})\gg 1
and the lower energy gauge
theory will be
{\cal N}=1\,\,SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})
SYM in the strong coupling regime,
with the scale factor of its gauge coupling determined as previously from the
matching

a_{-}(\mu=m_{Q,2}^{\rm pole})=\Bigl(\frac{m_{Q,2}^{\rm pole}}{\Lambda^{\prime}_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\frac{{\rm n}_{1}-(3N_{c}-2N_{F})}{N_{F}-N_{c}}\,>\,0}=a^{(\rm br1)}_{YM}(\mu=m_{Q,2}^{\rm pole})=\Bigl(\frac{m_{Q,2}^{\rm pole}}{\lambda_{YM}}\Bigr)^{3}\hskip 11.74988pt\rightarrow

\displaystyle\rightarrow\hskip 11.74988pt\lambda^{3}_{YM}=\Bigl(\langle{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(\rm br1)}\rangle\Bigr)^{3}=\Lambda_{Q}^{3}\Bigl(\frac{m_{Q}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\frac{{\rm n}_{2}-{\rm n}_{1}}{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}}\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\Bigr)^{\frac{{\rm n}_{1}}{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}}=\langle S\rangle_{\rm br1}.

(36.2.3)

as it should be, see (
36.1.1
),(
36.1.8
).

Because our main purpose in this section is to calculate the mass spectra in
the direct theory in cases with quarks in the strong coupling regime

a(\mu)\gg 1
, we consider below this case
m_{Q,2}^{\rm pole}\gg\Lambda^{\prime}_{Q}
only. This requires then, see (
36.1.9
),

\displaystyle\frac{\Lambda^{\prime}_{Q}}{m_{Q,2}^{\rm pole}}\ll 1\hskip 11.74988pt\rightarrow\hskip 11.74988pt\mu_{\Phi}\ll{\mu}_{\Phi,3}=\Lambda_{Q}\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{m_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\frac{3N_{c}-2N_{F}+{\rm n}_{1}}{2{\rm n}_{1}}\,<\,1}<\frac{\Lambda_{Q}^{2}}{m_{Q}}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt{\rm n}_{1}>3N_{c}-2N_{F}\,.

(36.2.4)

We distinguish then three regions. -

I)
6N_{c}/5\,<\,N_{F}\,<\,5N_{c}/4,\,\,(3N_{c}-2N_{F})\,<\,{\rm n}_{1}\,<\,N_{F}/2
. Here the hierarchies look as:

\,m_{1}\ll\mu_{\Phi,1}\ll\mu_{\Phi,2}\ll\mu_{\Phi,3}\ll\Lambda_{Q}^{2}/m_{Q}
, see
(
36.1.4
),(
36.1.12
),(
36.1.13
), while

1/2\,<\gamma_{Q}^{-}\,<1
.

a)  The region
m_{1}\ll\mu_{\Phi}\ll\mu_{\Phi,1}
. There are two complete
additional generations of all
N^{2}_{F}

\Phi
-particles,
\Phi_{1}^{1},\Phi_{1}^{2},\Phi_{2}^{1}
are relevant in the range of scales
\mu_{3}^{\rm pole}(\Phi^{1}_{1})<\mu<\mu_{2}^{\rm pole}(\Phi^{1}_{1})
, while
\Phi_{2}^{2}
is relevant
at
\mu_{3}^{\rm pole}(\Phi^{2}_{2})<\mu<\mu_{2}^{\rm pole}(\Phi^{2}_{2}),\,\,\mu_{2}^{\rm pole}(\Phi^{2}_{2})=\mu_{2}^{\rm pole}(\Phi^{1}_{1})=\mu_{o}^{\rm str}=\Lambda_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q}/m_{Q})^{{\overline{N}}_{c}/(5N_{c}-3N_{F})}
, see
(

),(

),(

).

b)  The region
\mu_{\Phi,1}\ll\mu_{\Phi}\ll\mu_{\Phi,2}
.
\,\,\,\Phi_{1}^{1},\Phi_{1}^{2},\Phi_{2}^{1}
are irrelevant at all scales
\mu<\mu^{\rm pole}_{1}(\Phi)\sim\mu_{\Phi}
. As for
\Phi_{2}^{2}

),(

\mu^{\rm pole}_{3}(\Phi_{2}^{2})<m_{Q,2}^{\rm pole}

. In this case all

\Phi
-particles are irrelevant at all scales
\mu<\mu^{\rm pole}_{1}(\Phi)\sim\mu_{\Phi}
.

II)
5N_{c}/4\,<\,N_{F}\,<\,4N_{c}/3,\,\,(5N_{c}-3N_{F})/2\,<\,{\rm n}_{1}\,<\,N_{F}/2
.
Here the hierarchies look as:
m_{1}\ll\mu_{\Phi,2}\ll\mu_{\Phi,1}\ll\mu_{\Phi,3}\ll\Lambda_{Q}^{2}/m_{Q}

.

. All fions are too

.

III)

.

and

. Because we consider only the

\mu_{\Phi}<\mu_{\Phi,3}
and
\mu_{\Phi,3}<\mu_{\Phi,1}
now, there will be

).

All formulas in special vacua with

37

Broken flavor symmetry.  Dual theory.

37.1

br1 and special vacua with
1\leq n_{1}<{\rm Min}\,(N_{F}/2,\,3N_{c}-2N_{F})

This dual theory is mostly in the IR free logarithmic regime in this case and
all logarithmic effects of the RG evolution will be ignored below for
simplicity, as before. All
N_{F}^{2}
fions
\Phi^{j}_{i}
have very large masses

\sim\mu_{\Phi}\gg\Lambda_{Q}
and can be integrated out from the beginning. The potentially
most important masses of dual quarks and gluons look here as follows,

\displaystyle\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,1}\sim\frac{\langle M_{1}\rangle_{\rm br1}=\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{\rm br1}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\sim\frac{m_{Q}\mu_{\Phi}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\gg\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,2}\sim\frac{\langle M_{2}\rangle_{\rm br1}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\sim\Lambda_{Q}\Bigl(\frac{m_{Q}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\frac{{\overline{N}}_{c}-n_{1}}{N_{c}-n_{1}}}\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\Bigr)^{\frac{n_{1}}{N_{c}-n_{1}}}\,,

(37.1.1)

\langle{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(\rm br1)}\rangle^{3}\equiv\langle S\rangle_{\rm br1}=\frac{\langle M_{1}\rangle\langle M_{2}\rangle}{\mu_{\Phi}}\sim\Lambda_{Q}^{3}\Bigl(\frac{m_{Q}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\frac{{\rm n}_{2}-n_{1}}{N_{c}-n_{1}}}\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\Bigr)^{\frac{n_{1}}{N_{c}-n_{1}}}\,,

while, see (
37.1.1
),

\displaystyle({\overline{\mu}}^{\,\rm pole}_{gl,2})^{2}\sim\langle{(\overline{q}}q)_{2}\rangle_{\rm br1}=\frac{\langle S\rangle_{\rm br1}\Lambda_{Q}}{\langle M_{2}\rangle_{\rm br1}}=\frac{\langle M_{1}\rangle_{\rm br1}\Lambda_{Q}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\sim m_{Q}\Lambda_{Q}\gg(\mu_{\rm gl,1})^{2}\,,

(37.1.2)

\displaystyle\Bigl(\frac{{\overline{\mu}}^{\,\rm pole}_{gl,2}}{\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,2}}\Bigr)^{2}\sim\Bigl(\frac{m_{Q}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\frac{3N_{c}-2N_{F}+n_{1}}{N_{c}-n_{1}}}\Bigl(\frac{\mu_{\Phi}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\frac{2n_{1}}{N_{c}-n_{1}}}\ll 1\,,

(37.1.3)

\Bigl(\frac{{\overline{\mu}}^{\,\rm pole}_{gl,2}}{\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,1}}\Bigr)^{2}\sim\frac{\Lambda_{Q}^{3}}{m_{Q}\mu_{\Phi}^{2}}<\frac{\Lambda_{Q}^{3}}{m_{Q}m_{1}^{2}}\sim\Bigl(\frac{m_{Q}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\frac{3N_{c}-2N_{F}}{N_{c}}}\ll 1\,,

\Bigl(\frac{\langle{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(\rm br1)\rangle}}{\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,2}}\Bigr)^{3}\sim\Bigl(\frac{{\overline{\mu}}^{\,\rm pole}_{gl,2}}{\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,2}}\Bigr)^{2}\sim\Bigl(\frac{m_{Q}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\frac{3N_{c}-2N_{F}+n_{1}}{N_{c}-n_{1}}}\Bigl(\frac{\mu_{\Phi}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\frac{2n_{1}}{N_{c}-n_{1}}}<\Bigl(\frac{m_{Q}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\frac{3N_{c}-2N_{F}-n_{1}}{N_{c}-n_{1}}}\ll 1\,.

Therefore, the overall phase is
Hq_{1}-Hq_{2}
, all dual quarks are
not higgsed but confined, the confinement originates from the unbroken

SU({\overline{N}}_{c})\,\,{\cal N}=1
SYM and so the scale of the confining string tension is

\sqrt{\sigma}\sim{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(\rm br1)}=\langle S\rangle^{1/3}_{\rm br1}
, see
(
37.1.1
).

At scales
\mu<\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,1}
all
{\overline{q}}^{1},q_{1}
quarks can be integrated out as heavy
ones and there remains
SU({\overline{N}}_{c})
gauge theory with
n_{2}
flavors of quarks
{\overline{q}}^{2},q_{2}
(and with
\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}^{\,\prime}=2N_{F}-3N_{c}+n_{1}\,<0\,)
and
N_{F}^{2}
mions
M^{i}_{j}
. Integrating out then these
quarks as heavy ones at
\mu<\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,2}
and then all
{\cal N}=1\,\,SU({\overline{N}}_{c})

SYM-gluons at
\mu<{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(\rm br1)}
via the VY-procedure, the lower energy
Lagrangian of mions looks as

\displaystyle K\sim{\rm Tr}\,\frac{M^{\dagger}M}{\Lambda_{Q}^{2}}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\hskip 11.74988pt{\cal W}={\cal W}_{M}+{\cal W}_{\rm non-pert}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt{\cal W}_{\rm non-pert}=-{\overline{N}}_{c}\Bigl(\frac{\det M}{\Lambda_{Q}^{\rm\rm{b_{o}}}}\Bigr)^{1/{\overline{N}}_{c}}\,,

(37.1.4)

{\cal W}_{M}=-\frac{1}{2\mu_{\Phi}}\Bigl[{\rm Tr}\,(M^{2})-\frac{1}{N_{c}}\Bigl({\rm Tr}\,M\Bigr)^{2}\Bigr].

From (
37.1.4
), the masses of mions
M^{i}_{j}
look as follows :

1)
n_{2}^{2}
mions
M_{2}^{2}
have masses

\displaystyle\mu^{\rm pole}(M_{2}^{2})\sim\frac{\langle M_{1}\rangle_{\rm br1}}{\langle M_{2}\rangle_{\rm br1}}\,\frac{\Lambda_{Q}^{2}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\sim\Lambda_{Q}\Bigl(\frac{m_{Q}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{N_{c}-n_{1}}}\Bigl(\frac{\mu_{\Phi}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\frac{n_{1}}{N_{c}-n_{1}}}\,,

(37.1.5)

\Bigl(\frac{\mu^{\rm pole}(M_{2}^{2})}{{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(\rm br1)}}\Bigr)^{3/2}\sim\Bigl(\frac{m_{Q}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\frac{3N_{c}-2N_{F}+n_{1}}{N_{c}-n_{1}}}\Bigl(\frac{\mu_{\Phi}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\frac{2n_{1}}{N_{c}-n_{1}}}<\Bigl(\frac{m_{Q}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\frac{3N_{c}-2N_{F}-n_{1}}{N_{c}-n_{1}}}\ll 1\,,

the main contribution to

in (

2)
n_{1}^{2}
mions

\displaystyle\mu^{\rm pole}(M_{1}^{1})\sim\frac{\Lambda_{Q}^{2}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\sim\frac{\langle M_{2}\rangle_{\rm br1}}{\langle M_{1}\rangle_{\rm br1}}\,\mu^{\rm pole}(M_{2}^{2})\sim\Bigl(\frac{m_{1}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\Bigr)^{\frac{N_{c}}{N_{c}-n_{1}}}\,\mu^{\rm pole}(M_{2}^{2})\ll\mu^{\rm pole}(M_{2}^{2})\,,

(37.1.6)

the main contribution to

2n_{1}n_{2}
mions

massless

\displaystyle\mu^{\rm pole}(M_{1}^{2})=\mu^{\rm pole}(M_{2}^{1})=0\,.

(37.1.7)

On the whole, the mass spectrum looks in this case as follows. -

1) There is a large number of hadrons made from weakly interacting

All quarks are weakly confined, i.e. the tension of the confining string

mions

37.2

br1 and special vacua with
3N_{c}-2N_{F}<n_{1}<N_{F}/2

The masses of dual quarks
\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,1}
and
\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,2}
are the same (with a logarithmic
accuracy), see (
37.1.1
). The difference is that the dual coupling
{\overline{a}}(\mu)
still decreased logarithmically with diminished scale at

\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,2}<\mu<\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,1}
at
1\leq n_{1}<{\rm Min}\,(N_{F}/2,\,3N_{c}-2N_{F})
, while now

\rm{\overline{b}}_{o}^{\,\prime}=3{\overline{N}}_{c}-n_{2}=n_{1}-(3N_{c}-2N_{F})>0
and
{\overline{a}}(\mu)
increases logarithmically with diminishing
scale at
\mu<\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,1}
until the dual theory enters the conformal regime at

\mu<{\tilde{\Lambda}}_{Q}
(if nothing prevents).
{\tilde{\Lambda}}_{Q}
is
determinedfrom the matching of dual couplings at
\mu=\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,1}
, see
(
37.1.1
),(
36.2.2
),

\displaystyle({\tilde{\Lambda}}_{Q})^{3{\overline{N}}_{c}-n_{2}}=\Lambda_{Q}^{3{\overline{N}}_{c}-N_{F}}(\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,1})^{n_{1}}\hskip 11.74988pt\rightarrow\hskip 11.74988pt{\tilde{\Lambda}}_{Q}=\Lambda_{Q}\Bigl(\frac{m_{Q}\mu_{\Phi}}{\Lambda_{Q}^{2}}\Bigr)^{\frac{n_{1}}{n_{1}-(3N_{c}-2N_{F})}\,>\,0}=\Lambda^{\prime}_{Q}\ll\Lambda_{Q}\,.

(37.2.1)

Therefore, we need
\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,2}>\Lambda^{\prime}_{Q}
in order not to enter
the conformal regime, this requires, see (
37.2.1
),(
37.1.1
),

\displaystyle\frac{\Lambda^{\prime}_{Q}}{\mu^{\rm pole}_{q,2}}=\Bigl(\frac{\mu_{\Phi}}{\mu_{\Phi,3}}\Bigr)^{\frac{n_{1}}{(n_{1}-3N_{c}+2N_{F})}\frac{2{\overline{N}}_{c}}{(N_{c}-n_{1})}}\ll 1\,\rightarrow\,\mu_{\Phi}\,\ll\,\mu_{\Phi,3}

(37.2.2)

as in the direct theory, see (
36.2.4
). The masses
\mu^{\rm pole}(M_{2}^{2})

and
\mu^{\rm pole}(M_{1}^{1})\ll\mu^{\rm pole}(M_{2}^{2})
remain as in
(

),(

),

\displaystyle\frac{\mu^{\rm pole}(M_{2}^{2})}{{\Lambda}_{SYM}^{(\rm br1)}}\sim\Bigl(\frac{\mu_{\Phi}}{\mu_{\Phi,3}}\Bigr)^{\frac{4n_{1}}{3(N_{c}-n_{1})}}\ll 1\hskip 11.74988pt{\rm at}\hskip 11.74988pt\mu_{\Phi}\ll\mu_{\Phi,3}=\Lambda_{Q}\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{m_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\frac{3N_{c}-2N_{F}+n_{1}}{2n_{1}}\,<\,1}<\frac{\Lambda_{Q}^{2}}{m_{Q}}\,.

(37.2.3)

All formulas in special vacua with
{\rm n}_{1}={\overline{N}}_{c},\,{\rm n}_{2}=N_{c}
can be obtained simply

{\rm n}_{1}={\overline{N}}_{c}

In dual br2 vacua with

38

Conclusions to Part II

This Part IIc continues previous study in
[
25
,
17
]

of
{\cal N}=1
SQCD-type theories with additional colorless but flavored
fields. But considered this time is the region
N_{c}<N_{F}<3N_{c}/2
. In this
region,  the UV free direct
SU(N_{c})
theory with light quarks enters
smoothly
at
\mu<\Lambda_{Q}
to the (very) strongly coupled regime with the coupling

a(\mu\ll\Lambda_{Q})\gg 1
(see Introduction, section 7 in
[
11
]
and Conclusions
in
[
27
]
for additional arguments). The mass spectra were calculated
in
[
26
]
in numerous different vacua. The calculations were performed
within the dynamical scenario introduced in
[
20
]
. This scenario assumes
that quarks
in such
{\cal N}=1
SQCD-type theories can be in two
standard
phases
only : these are either the HQ (heavy quark) phase where they are confined,
or the Higgs phase. The word
standard
implies here also that, in such
theories without elementary colored adjoint scalars, no
additional

46
46

46

i.e. in addition to Nambu-Goldstone particles due to spontaneously broken
global flavor symmetry

parametrically light solitons (e.g. magnetic monopoles or dyons) are formed at
those lower scales
\mu\ll\Lambda_{Q}
where quarks decouple as heavy or are higgsed.

47
47

47

Besides, it is worth noting that the appearance of additional parametrically
light composite solitons will influence the ’t Hooft triangles.

Similarly to previous studies in
[
25
,
17
]
of these theories
within the conformal window at
3N_{c}/2<N_{F}<3N_{c}
, it is shown here that, due
to the strong powerlike RG evolution at scales
\mu<\Lambda_{Q}
in the direct theory, the
seemingly heavy and dynamically irrelevant fields
\Phi^{j}_{i}
can become light
and there appear then two additional generations of light
\Phi
-particles with

\mu_{2,3}^{\rm pole}(\Phi)\ll\Lambda_{Q}
.

In parallel, were calculated the mass spectra of IR free and logarithmically
weakly coupled at
\mu<\Lambda_{Q}
dual
\,SU({\overline{N}}_{c}=N_{F}-N_{c})
theory proposed by
Seiberg.

On the whole for the Part II, the comparison have shown that the mass spectra of the
direct and dual theories are
parametrically different, in general, so that these
two theories are not equivalent
.

As it is seen from the results in the whole Part II, the use of the dynamical scenario
from
[
20
]
leads to the results for the mass spectra which look
self-consistent. In other words, no internal inconsistences appeared in all
cases considered and no contradictions were found with all previously proven results.
It is worth to recall also that this dynamical scenario used
in this article satisfies all those tests which were used as checks of the
Seiberg proposal about the equivalence of the direct and dual theories.
The parametric difference of mass spectra of direct and dual theories shows,
in particular, that all these tests, although necessary, may well be insufficient.

39

Appendices 1 and 2

Appendix 1 .  Anomalous dimensions in the strong coupling regime

The purpose of this Appendix is to find the anomalous dimensions of quarks and
colorless but flavored fields
\Phi^{j}_{i}
in the theory (
31.1.5
),(
31.1.6
)
in the strong coupling regime in the range of scales where they are dynamically
relevant, i.e. their running masses at the scale
\mu
are smaller than
\mu
.

Note first that there is the first generation of all
N_{F}^{2}
fions
\Phi_{i}^{j}

with masses
\mu_{1}^{\rm pole}(\Phi)\sim\mu_{\Phi}\gg\Lambda_{Q}
. As shown in section 5 of

[
17
]
, the anomalous dimension
\gamma^{(+)}_{\Phi}
of fions in the
rangeof scales
\mu_{o}^{\rm str}<\mu<\Lambda_{Q}
where they are still heavy and dynamically
irrelevant is:

\gamma^{(+)}_{\Phi}=\,-\,2\gamma^{(+)}_{Q},\,\,\gamma^{(+)}_{Q}=(2N_{c}-N_{F})/{\overline{N}}_{c}
.

Besides, there is also the second generation of all
N_{F}^{2}
fions with masses,
see section 17 and (
31.1.7
),

\displaystyle{\hskip-14.22636pt}\mu_{2}^{\rm pole}(\Phi)\sim\mu_{o}^{\rm str}\sim\Lambda_{Q}\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\Bigr)^{\frac{1}{2\gamma^{(+)}_{Q}-1}}=\Lambda_{Q}\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{\mu_{\Phi}}\Bigr)^{\frac{{\overline{N}}_{c}}{5N_{c}-3N_{F}}}\ll\Lambda_{Q},

(39.1)

and all
N_{F}^{2}
”heavy” fions become effectively massless and dynamically
relevant in the direct theory (
31.1.5
),(
31.1.6
) at
\mu<\mu_{o}^{\rm str}
.

a) Now, as for the anomalous dimension
{\tilde{\gamma}}_{Q}^{(+)}
of strongly
coupled quarks in the range
m_{Q,1}^{\rm pole}<\mu<\mu_{o}^{\rm str}
where the fions became relevant. We use first the approach

[
11
]
(see section 7 therein). For this, we have to introduce the dual
theory which has the same ’t Hooft triangles (in a given range of scales) as
the   direct theory.

In the range
\mu_{o}^{\rm str}\ll\mu\ll\Lambda_{Q}
where the all fields
\Phi^{j}_{i}
are too heavy
and dynamically irrelevant in both the direct and dual theories , the dual theory
can be taken as (
14.2.2
), see section 14.2. The effectively massless particles in
this range   of scales in the direct theory are all quarks and gluons, while in the dual
one these are the dual quarks and gluons and mions
M^{i}_{j}
. As
shown in
[
5
]
, all ’t Hooft triangles will be the same in these two
theories.

Now, in the approach
[
11
]
, we equate two NSVZ
\,{\widehat{\beta}}_{ext}
-
functions of the external baryon and
SU(N_{F})_{L}
- flavor vector fields in
the   direct and dual theories,

\displaystyle\frac{d}{d\,\ln\mu}\,\frac{2\pi}{\alpha_{ext}}={\widehat{\beta}}_{ext}=-\frac{2\pi}{\alpha^{2}_{ext}}\,\beta_{ext}=\sum_{i}T_{i}\,\bigl(1+\gamma_{i}\bigr)\,,

(39.2)

where the sum runs over all fields which are effectively massless at scales
considered, the unity in the brackets is due to one-loop contributions while
the   anomalous dimensions
\gamma_{i}
of fields represent all higher-loop effects,

T_{i}
are the coefficients. It is worth noting that these general NSVZ forms
(
39.2
) of the external ”flavored”
\widehat{\beta}_{ext}
-functions are
independent of the kind of massless perturbative regime of the internal gauge
theory, i.e. whether it is conformal, or the strong coupling or IR free
one. For the baryon charge (
39.2
) looks as

\displaystyle N_{F}N_{c}\,\Bigl(B_{Q}=1\Bigr)^{2}\,(1+\gamma^{(+)}_{Q})=N_{F}{\overline{N}}_{c}\,\Bigl(B_{q}=\frac{N_{c}}{{\overline{N}}_{c}}\Bigr)^{2}\,(1+\gamma_{q})\,.

(39.3)

In (
39.3
): the left-hand side is from the direct theory while the
right-hand side is from the dual one. The dual theory is in the
IR free logarithmic regime at
\mu\ll\Lambda_{Q}
with the dual coupling
{\overline{a}}(\mu)\ll 1
and
\gamma_{q}\sim{\overline{a}}\rightarrow 0
. Then from (
39.3
), see
section 7 in
[
11
]

\displaystyle\gamma^{(+)}_{Q}=\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{N_{F}-N_{c}},\hskip 11.74988pt\frac{d\,a(\mu\ll\Lambda_{Q})}{d\log\mu}=\beta_{NSVZ}(a\gg 1)=\frac{a^{2}(\mu)}{a(\mu)-1}\,\frac{\rm{b_{o}}-N_{F}\gamma_{Q}^{(+)}}{N_{c}}\rightarrow-\,\nu^{(+)}\,a(\mu),

(39.4)

a(\mu_{o}^{\rm str}\ll\mu\ll\Lambda_{Q})\sim(\Lambda_{Q}/\mu)^{\nu^{(+)}}\gg 1,\,\,\,\,\,\,\nu^{(+)}=\frac{3N_{c}-2N_{F}}{N_{F}-N_{c}}\,>\,0,\,\,\,\rm{b_{o}}=3N_{c}-N_{F}\,.

For the flavor charge (
39.2
) looks as

\displaystyle N_{c}(1+\gamma^{(+)}_{Q})={\overline{N}}_{c}(1+\gamma_{q})+N_{F}(1+\gamma_{M})\,.

(39.5)

Both
\gamma_{q}
and
\gamma_{M}
are logarithmically small at
\mu\ll\Lambda_{Q}
and
(
39.5
) is incompatible with (
39.4
) (and with the NSVZ

\beta
-function, see section 7 in
[
11
]
). Therefore, (
39.2
) for the
flavor charge will not be used (here and below). (But within the conformal
window
3N_{c}/2<N_{F}<2N_{c}
both (
39.3
) and (
39.5
) are fulfilled).

b) Now, for the range
m_{Q,1}^{\rm pole}\ll\mu\ll\mu_{o}^{\rm str}
where the fions
in   the direct theory became relevant in the strong coupling regime
a(\mu)\gg 1
,
the dual theory with the same ’t Hooft triangles (now
with effectively massless fions
\Phi
in the direct theory) can be taken as (
3.0.1
) but

without the mion fields

M^{i}_{j}
(i.e. only massless dual quarks
{\overline{q}}^{\,j},q_{i}
and
SU({\overline{N}}_{c})
dual gluons). But because the baryon charge of fions

\Phi^{j}_{i}
and mions
M^{i}_{j}
is zero, (
39.3
)
(
39.4
) remain the same, i.e. the anomalous dimension

{\widetilde{\gamma}}^{\,(+)}_{Q}
of quarks
{\overline{Q}},Q
remains the same after fions
became relevant,
{\widetilde{\gamma}}^{\,(+)}_{Q}=\gamma^{(+)}_{Q}=(2N_{c}-N_{F})/{\overline{N}}_{c}
.

c) Because (
39.2
) for the flavor charge is not fulfilled, the
above   method does not allow to find the anomalous dimension

{\widetilde{\gamma}}^{\,(+)}_{\Phi}
at
\mu<\mu_{o}^{\rm str}
where the fions become effectively
massless and dynamically relevant. Therefore, on the example of the strongly
coupled theory (
31.1.5
),(
31.1.6
), we present now independent
way   to connect the
anomalous dimensions of quarks and
\Phi
,
\,{\widetilde{\gamma}}^{\,(+)}_{Q}
and

{\widetilde{\gamma}}^{\,(+)}_{\Phi}
, at those scales where
\Phi
became relevant, i.e.
at
\mu<\mu_{o}^{\rm str}
. It follows from the internal self consistency when the quarks

{\overline{Q}}_{1},Q^{1}
decouple as heavy ones at scales
\mu<m_{Q,1}^{\rm pole}
in
the direct theory, while all
N_{F}^{2}
fions
\Phi^{j}_{i}
still remain
effectively massless in the large range of scales
\mu^{\rm pole}_{3}(\Phi^{1}_{1})<\mu<\mu_{o}^{\rm str},\,\,\mu^{\rm pole}_{3}(\Phi^{1}_{1})\ll m^{\rm pole}_{Q,1}\ll\mu_{o}^{\rm str}
.

The Lagrangian of the direct theory, which is in the HQ (heavy
quark) phase with
m_{Q,1}^{\rm pole}\gg m_{Q,2}^{\rm pole}
,
has the form at the scale
\mu=m_{Q,1}^{\rm pole}\ll\Lambda_{Q}

K=K_{\Phi}+K_{Q}=z^{(+)}_{\Phi}{\rm Tr}\,(\Phi^{\dagger}\Phi)+z^{(+)}_{Q}{\rm Tr}\,(Q^{\dagger}Q+Q\rightarrow{\overline{Q}})={\rm Tr}\,(\delta{\Phi_{R}}^{\dagger}\delta{\Phi_{R}})+{\rm Tr}\,({Q_{R}}^{\dagger}{Q_{R}}+Q_{R}\rightarrow{\overline{Q}}_{R}),

z^{(+)}_{Q}=z^{(+)}_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q},m_{Q,1}^{\rm pole})=z^{(+)}_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu_{o}^{\rm str})\,{\widetilde{z}}^{\,\,(+)}_{Q}(\mu_{o}^{\rm str},m_{Q,1}^{\rm pole})=\Bigl(\frac{\mu_{o}^{\rm str}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\gamma^{(+)}_{Q}}\Bigl(\frac{m_{Q,1}^{\rm pole}}{\mu_{o}^{\rm str}}\Bigr)^{{\widetilde{\gamma}}^{\,(+)}_{Q}}\,,

\displaystyle\hskip 11.74988ptz^{(+)}_{\Phi}=z^{(+)}_{\Phi}(\Lambda_{Q},m_{Q,1}^{\rm pole})=z^{(+)}_{\Phi}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu_{o}^{\rm str})\,{\widetilde{z}}^{\,\,(+)}_{\Phi}(\mu_{o}^{\rm str},m_{Q,1}^{\rm pole})=\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{Q}}{\mu_{o}^{\rm str}}\Bigr)^{2\gamma^{(+)}_{Q}}\Bigl(\frac{m_{Q,1}^{\rm pole}}{\mu_{o}^{\rm str}}\Bigr)^{{\widetilde{\gamma}}^{\,(+)}_{\Phi}}\,,

(39.6)

\displaystyle m_{Q,1}^{\rm pole}=\frac{\langle m_{Q,1}^{\rm tot}\rangle}{z^{(+)}_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q},m_{Q,1}^{\rm pole})},\,\,\langle m_{Q,1}^{\rm tot}\rangle=\frac{\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle}{\mu_{\Phi}}\sim m_{Q},\,\,m_{Q}^{\rm tot}=m_{Q}-\Phi=\langle m_{Q}^{\rm tot}\rangle-\delta\Phi,\,\,\langle\delta\Phi\rangle=0,\,\,\,\,

(39.7)

where
\Phi_{R}
and
Q_{R}
are the canonically normalized fields and

\delta\Phi_{R}
is a pure quantum fluctuation,

\displaystyle{\cal W}_{\rm matter}={\cal W}_{\Phi}+{\rm Tr}\,({\overline{Q}}\,m_{Q}^{\rm tot}Q)\,,

(39.8)

see
[
26
]
for all details.

At
\mu<m_{Q,1}^{\rm pole}
all quarks
Q^{1},{\overline{Q}}_{1}
decouple and can be
integrated out as heavy ones. As a result, there will appear a series of higher
dimension operators in D-terms, e.g.

\displaystyle O_{n}\sim a_{f}^{(1)}\delta\Phi_{R}^{\dagger}\delta\Phi_{R}\Biggl[a^{(1)}_{f}\frac{\delta\Phi_{R}^{\dagger}\delta\Phi_{R}}{\Bigl(m_{Q,1}^{\rm pole}\Bigr)^{2}}\,\Biggr]^{\rm n}\,,\hskip 11.74988pta^{(1)}_{f}=\frac{a_{f}(\mu=\Lambda_{Q})\sim 1}{z^{(+)}_{\Phi}\,(z^{(+)}_{Q})^{2}}\,,

(39.9)

where
a^{(1)}_{f}=a_{f}(\mu=m_{Q,1}^{\rm pole})
is the Yukawa coupling at the
scale
\mu=m_{Q,1}^{\rm pole}
. These terms originate e.g. from the expansion in
powers of
\delta{{\cal M}_{R}}
of the heavy quark loop integrated over the
non-parametric interval of momenta
p\sim m_{Q,1}^{\rm pole}
,

\displaystyle\Delta_{\Phi}\sim a^{(1)}_{f}\delta\Phi_{R}^{\dagger}\delta\Phi_{R}\int d^{4}p\,[\,p^{2}+{\cal M}^{\dagger}{\cal M}\,]^{-2},\,\,{\cal M}=m_{Q,1}^{\rm pole}\Biggl(1-\sqrt{a^{(1)}_{f}}\frac{\delta{\Phi_{R}}}{m_{Q,1}^{\rm pole}}\Biggr),\,\,\langle{\cal M}\rangle=m_{Q,1}^{\rm pole}.

(39.10)

When propagating in the loop with the momentum ”k”, the canonical fields

\delta{\Phi_{R}}\sim k
. Therefore, in order that heavy quarks
{\overline{Q}}_{1},Q^{1}

really completely decouple as it should be, the contributions from

O_{\rm n}
in (
39.9
) have to be small at momenta
k<m_{Q,1}^{\rm pole}
in comparison with the canonical Kahler term

\delta{\Phi_{R}}^{\dagger}\delta{\Phi_{R}}
. Then, because the expansion
(
39.9
) of (
39.10
) breaks down at larger momenta
>m_{Q,1}^{\rm pole}
(in any case, the running quark mass diminishes very quickly with
increasing momentum), all terms
O_{\rm n}/[\delta{\Phi_{R}}^{\dagger}\delta{\Phi_{R}}]
will be
O(1)
at momenta
\sim m_{Q,1}^{\rm pole}
.
For this, it should be parametrically
a^{(1)}_{f}\sim 1/[z^{(+)}_{\Phi}(z^{(+)}_{Q})^{2}]\sim 1
, see (
39.6
),(
39.7
),(
39.9
),

\displaystyle a^{(1)}_{f}=O(1)\,\,\rightarrow\,\,z^{(+)}_{\Phi}\,\Bigl(z^{(+)}_{Q}\,\Bigr)^{2}=z^{(+)}_{\Phi}(\mu_{o}^{\rm str},m_{Q,1}^{\rm pole})\,\Bigl(z^{(+)}_{Q}(\mu_{o}^{\rm str},m_{Q,1}^{\rm pole})\,\Bigr)^{2}\sim 1\,\,\rightarrow\,\,{\widetilde{\gamma}}^{\,(+)}_{\Phi}=-2\,{\widetilde{\gamma}}^{\,(+)}_{Q}\,.

(39.11)

(
39.11
) ensures then that corrections from
O_{\rm n}
will be small at
momenta
k<m_{Q,1}^{\rm pole}
, so that quarks
{\overline{Q}}_{1},Q^{1}
really decouple
completely.

Therefore,
\gamma_{\Phi}=-2\gamma_{Q}
not only at
\mu_{o}^{\rm str}<\mu<\Lambda_{Q}
where
fions
\Phi^{j}_{i}
were irrelevant, but also
{\widetilde{\gamma}}_{\Phi}=-2\,{\widetilde{\gamma}}_{Q}

at lower scales
\mu<\mu_{o}^{\rm str}
where they became relevant.

d) And finally, we present also independent reasonings leading to the
same   results. First, we point out that the gauge coupling
a(\mu\ll\Lambda_{Q})\gg 1
entered
already into a strong coupling regime, while

\gamma^{(+)}_{\Phi}=-2\gamma^{(+)}_{Q}
at
\mu_{o}^{\rm str}<\mu<\Lambda_{Q}
where the fions are
still irrelevant
[
11
]
. Therefore, the gauge and Yukawa couplings look at

\mu=\mu_{o}^{\rm str}\ll\Lambda_{Q}
as, see (
39.4
),

\displaystyle a(\mu=\mu_{o}^{\rm str})=(\Lambda_{Q}/\mu_{o}^{\rm str})^{\nu^{(+)}\,>\,0}\gg 1,\hskip 11.74988pta_{f}(\mu=\mu_{o}^{\rm str})=\frac{a_{f}(\mu=\Lambda_{Q})=1}{z^{(+)}_{\Phi}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu_{o}^{\rm str})[\,z^{(+)}_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu_{o}^{\rm str})\,]^{2}}\sim 1\,.

(39.12)

Consider now the Feynman diagrams in the strongly coupled direct theory
contributing to the renormalization factors
z_{\Phi}(\mu)
and

z_{Q}(\mu)
at
m^{\rm pole}_{Q,1}\ll\mu\ll\mu_{o}^{\rm str}
where both the quarks and
fions
\Phi
are effectively massless. Order by order in the perturbation theory
the extra loop with the exchange of the field
\Phi
is
a_{f}(\mu_{o}^{\rm str})/a(\mu_{o}^{\rm str})\sim(\mu_{o}^{\rm str}/\Lambda_{Q})^{\nu^{(+)}\,>\,0}\ll 1
times smaller than the extra loop but with
the exchange of gluon.

In all cases with a resummation of perturbative series, a standard assumption
is
that the leading contribution to the sum originates from summation of leading
terms at each order. With this assumption, we can neglect all exchanges of

\Phi
in comparison with those of gluons, order by order. Therefore, the fact
that the fields
\Phi^{j}_{i}
became relevant at
\mu<\mu_{o}^{\rm str}
is really of no
importance for the RG-evolution, so that both
{\widetilde{\gamma}}^{\,\rm(+)}_{Q}
and

{\widetilde{\gamma}}^{\,(+)}_{\Phi}

remain the same
at
m^{\rm pole}_{Q,1}\ll\mu\ll\mu_{o}^{\rm str}
as they were at
\mu_{o}^{\rm str}\ll\mu\ll\Lambda_{Q}
when fions were
irrelevant:

{\widetilde{\gamma}}^{\,\rm(+)}_{Q}=\gamma^{\rm(+)}_{Q},\,\,{\widetilde{\gamma}}^{\,(+)}_{\Phi}=\gamma^{(+)}_{\Phi}=\,-2\,\gamma^{\rm(+)}_{Q}
(i.e. the Yukawa coupling
a_{f}(\mu)
still stays
intact at
a_{f}(\mu)\sim 1
also at
m^{\rm pole}_{Q,1}\ll\mu\ll\mu_{o}^{\rm str}
, as it was
at
\mu_{o}^{\rm str}\ll\mu\ll\Lambda_{Q}
). The equality
{\widetilde{\gamma}}^{\,\rm(+)}_{Q}=\gamma^{\rm(+)}_{Q}
agrees with ”
{\bf b}
” above, while

{\widetilde{\gamma}}^{\,(+)}_{\Phi}=\,-2\,{\widetilde{\gamma}}^{\,\rm(+)}_{Q}
agrees with
”
{\bf c}
” above.

It is also worth to remind that, once the fions
\Phi^{j}_{i}
become effectively
massless and dynamically relevant with respect to internal interactions, they
simultaneously begin to contribute to the ’t Hooft triangles

The gluon contributions into the RG evolution of quarks at
m_{Q,1}^{\rm pole}<\mu<\Lambda_{Q}
were already accounted in (
39.6
). Besides, because quarks

Q^{1},{\overline{Q}}_{1}
are confined, they form a set of color neutral strongly coupled
hadrons with the characteristic scale of hadron masses
{\overline{M}}_{\rm hadr}\sim m_{Q,1}^{\rm pole}
. Therefore, gluons with momenta
\lesssim m_{Q,1}^{\rm pole}
effectively decouple from these quarks, and for this reason gluons are
absent in (
39.10
). On the other hand, there are no reasons to decouple
for the colorless fions
\Phi^{j}_{i}
.

Appendix 2 .  Broken
\mathcal{N}=2
SQCD

We consider in this Appendix 2
{\cal N}=2
SQCD with

SU(N_{c})
colors,
1\leq N_{F}\ <2N_{c}

flavors of light quarks, the scale factor
\Lambda_{2}
of the gauge coupling,
and with
{\cal N}=2
broken down to
{\cal N}=1
by the large mass parameter

\mu_{\rm x}\gg\Lambda_{2}
of the adjoint field
X=X^{A}T^{A},\,{\rm Tr}\,(T^{A}T^{B})=\delta^{AB}/2
. At large
\mu\gg\mu_{\rm x}
the Lagrangian looks as
(the exponents with gluons are implied in the Kahler term K)

\displaystyle K=\frac{1}{g_{2}^{2}(\mu,\Lambda_{2})}{\rm Tr}\,(X^{\dagger}X)+{\rm Tr}\,({\textbf{Q}}^{\dagger}\textbf{Q}+\textbf{Q}\rightarrow\overline{\textbf{Q}})\,,\hskip 11.74988ptg_{2}^{2}=4\pi\alpha_{2}\,.

(39.13)

{\cal W}=\frac{2\pi}{\alpha_{2}(\mu,\Lambda_{2})}S+\mu_{\rm x}{\rm Tr}\,(X^{2})+\sqrt{2}\,{\rm Tr}\,(\overline{\textbf{Q}}X\textbf{Q})+m\,{\rm Tr}\,(\overline{\textbf{Q}}\textbf{Q}).

The Konishi anomalies look here as

\langle X^{A}\frac{\partial{\cal W}}{\partial X^{A}}\rangle=\mu_{\rm x}\langle X^{A}X^{A}\rangle+{\rm Tr}\langle J^{A}X^{A}\rangle=2N_{c}\langle S\rangle,\hskip 11.74988ptJ^{A,i}_{j}={\sqrt{2}}\,(\overline{\textbf{Q}}_{j}T^{A}\textbf{Q}^{i})\,,\hskip 11.74988pt{\rm Tr}\,\Bigl(T^{A}T^{B}\Bigr)=\frac{1}{2}\,\delta^{AB}\,,

\langle\overline{\textbf{Q}}_{i}\frac{\partial{\cal W}}{\partial\overline{\textbf{Q}}_{i}}\rangle=\langle J^{A,i}_{i}X^{A}\rangle+m\langle\overline{\textbf{Q}}_{i}\textbf{Q}^{i}\rangle=\langle S\rangle\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\rm{no\,\,\,summation\,\,\,over\,\,\,i}\,.

From these

2\langle{\rm Tr}\,X^{2}\rangle=\langle X^{A}X^{A}\rangle=\frac{1}{\mu_{\rm x}}\Bigl[(2N_{c}-N_{F})\langle S\rangle+m\langle{\rm Tr}\,\overline{\textbf{Q}}\textbf{Q}\rangle\Bigr]\,.

The running mass of
X
is
\mu_{\rm x}(\mu)=g_{2}^{2}(\mu)\mu_{\rm x}
, so that at scales

\mu<\mu^{\rm pole}_{\rm x}=g_{2}^{2}(\mu^{\rm pole}_{\rm x})\mu_{\rm x}
the field
X
decouples
from the dynamics and the RG evolution becomes those of
{\cal N}=1
SQCD. The
matching of
{\cal N}=2
and
{\cal N}=1
couplings at
\mu=\mu^{\rm pole}_{\rm x}

looks as (
\Lambda_{2}
and
\Lambda_{Q}
are the scale factors of
{\cal N}=2
and

{\cal N}=1
gauge couplings,
\Lambda_{Q}
is held fixed when
\mu_{\rm x}\gg\Lambda_{Q}
is varied,

{\rm b}_{2}=2N_{c}-N_{F}\,,\,\rm{b_{o}}=3N_{c}-N_{F}
)

\frac{2\pi}{\alpha_{2}(\mu=\mu^{\rm pole}_{\rm x},\Lambda_{2})}=\frac{2\pi}{\alpha(\mu=\mu^{\rm pole}_{\rm x},\Lambda_{Q})}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\mu_{\rm x}\gg\Lambda_{Q}\gg\Lambda_{2}\,,

\displaystyle{\rm b}_{2}\ln\frac{\mu^{\rm pole}_{\rm x}}{\Lambda_{2}}=\rm{b_{o}}\ln\frac{\mu^{\rm pole}_{\rm x}}{\Lambda_{Q}}-N_{F}\ln z_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu^{\rm pole}_{\rm x})+N_{c}\ln\frac{1}{g^{2}(\mu=\mu^{\rm pole}_{\rm x})}\,,

(39.14)

\Lambda_{Q}^{\rm{b_{o}}}=\frac{\Lambda^{{\rm b}_{2}}_{2}\mu^{N_{c}}_{\rm x}}{z^{N_{F}}_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu^{\rm pole}_{\rm x})},\hskip 11.74988ptz_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu=\mu^{\rm pole}_{\rm x})\sim\Bigl(\ln\frac{\mu^{\rm pole}_{\rm x}}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{\frac{N_{c}}{\rm{b_{o}}}}\gg 1.

Although the field
X
becomes too heavy and does not propagate any more at

\mu<\mu^{\rm pole}_{\rm x}
, the loops of light quarks and gluons are still
active at
\Lambda_{Q}<\mu<\mu^{\rm pole}_{\rm x}
if the next largest physical mass
\mu_{H}

is below
\Lambda_{Q}
. These loops induce then to the field
X
at
\mu=\Lambda_{Q}\ll\mu^{\rm pole}_{\rm x}
a non-trivial logarithmic renormalization factor
z_{X}(\mu^{\rm pole}_{\rm x},\Lambda_{Q})=z^{-1}_{X}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu^{\rm pole}_{\rm x})\ll 1
.

Therefore, finally, at scales
\Lambda_{Q}\ll\mu\ll\mu^{\rm pole}_{\rm x}
the Lagrangian of the
broken
{\cal N}=2
- theory with
0<N_{F}<2N_{c}
can
be written as

\displaystyle K=\frac{z_{X}(\mu^{\rm pole}_{\rm x},\mu)}{g^{2}(\mu^{\rm pole}_{\rm x})}\,{\rm Tr}\,(X^{\dagger}X)+z_{Q}(\mu^{\rm pole}_{\rm x},\mu)\,{\rm Tr}\,({\textbf{Q}}^{\dagger}\textbf{Q}+\textbf{Q}\rightarrow\overline{\textbf{Q}})\,,

(39.15)

{\cal W}=\frac{2\pi}{\alpha(\mu,\Lambda_{Q})}S+\mu_{\rm x}{\rm Tr}\,(X^{2})+\sqrt{2}\,{\rm Tr}\,(\overline{\textbf{Q}}X\textbf{Q})+m\,{\rm Tr}\,(\overline{\textbf{Q}}\textbf{Q})\,.

\displaystyle z_{X}(\mu^{\rm pole}_{\rm x},\mu)\sim\Biggl(\,\frac{\ln\,(\mu/\Lambda_{Q})}{\ln\,(\mu^{\rm pole}_{\rm x}/\Lambda_{Q})}\,\Biggr)^{{\rm b}_{2}/\rm{b_{o}}}\ll 1\,,

(39.16)

z_{Q}(\mu^{\rm pole}_{\rm x},\mu)=z_{Q}(\mu^{\rm pole}_{\rm x},\Lambda_{Q})z_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu),\hskip 11.74988ptz_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu)\sim\Bigl(\ln\frac{\mu}{\Lambda_{Q}}\Bigr)^{N_{c}/\rm{b_{o}}}\gg 1\,.

In all cases when the field
X
remains too heavy and dynamically irrelevant,
it can be integrated out in (
39.15
) and one obtains

\displaystyle K=z_{Q}(\mu^{\rm pole}_{\rm x},\mu)\,{\rm Tr}\,({\textbf{Q}}^{\dagger}\textbf{Q}+\textbf{Q}\rightarrow\overline{\textbf{Q}})\,,

(39.17)

{\cal W}_{Q}=\frac{2\pi}{\alpha(\mu,\Lambda_{Q})}S+m\,{\rm Tr}(\overline{\textbf{Q}}\textbf{Q})-\frac{1}{2\mu_{\rm x}}\Biggl({\rm Tr}\,(\overline{\textbf{Q}}\textbf{Q})^{2}-\frac{1}{N_{c}}\Bigl({\rm Tr}\,\overline{\textbf{Q}}\textbf{Q}\Bigr)^{2}\Biggr).

Now we redefine the normalization of quarks fields

\displaystyle\textbf{Q}=\frac{1}{z^{1/2}_{Q}(\mu^{\rm pole}_{\rm x},\Lambda_{Q})}\,Q\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\overline{\textbf{Q}}=\frac{1}{z^{1/2}_{Q}(\mu^{\rm pole}_{\rm x},\Lambda_{Q})}\,{\overline{Q}}\,,

(39.18)

\displaystyle K=z_{Q}(\Lambda_{Q},\mu){\rm Tr}\Bigl(\,Q^{\dagger}Q+(Q\rightarrow{\overline{Q}})\,\Bigr),\hskip 11.74988pt{\cal W}=\frac{2\pi}{\alpha(\mu,\Lambda_{Q})}S+W_{Q}\,,

(39.19)

\displaystyle{\cal W}_{Q}=\frac{m}{z_{Q}(\mu^{\rm pole}_{\rm x},\Lambda_{Q})}\,{\rm Tr}({\overline{Q}}Q)-\frac{1}{2z^{2}_{Q}(\mu^{\rm pole}_{\rm x},\Lambda_{Q})\mu_{\rm x}}\Biggl({\rm Tr}\,({\overline{Q}}Q)^{2}-\frac{1}{N_{c}}\Bigl({\rm Tr}\,{\overline{Q}}Q\Bigr)^{2}\Biggr).

(39.20)

Comparing this with (
14.1.3
) and choosing

\displaystyle\frac{m}{z_{Q}(\mu^{\rm pole}_{\rm x},\Lambda_{Q})}=m_{Q}\ll\Lambda_{Q}\,,\hskip 11.74988ptz^{2}_{Q}(\mu^{\rm pole}_{\rm x},\Lambda_{Q})\mu_{\rm x}=\mu_{\Phi}\gg\Lambda_{Q}

(39.21)

it is seen that with this matching the
\Phi
- theory and the broken
{\cal N}=2
SQCD, both normalized canonically at
\mu=\Lambda_{Q}
, will be equivalent.

Therefore, at large
\mu_{\rm x}\gg\Lambda_{2}
and until both
X
and
\Phi
fields
remain dynamically irrelevant, all results obtained above for the
\Phi
-
theory will be applicable to the broken
{\cal N}=2
SQCD as well. Besides, the

\Phi
and
X
fields remain dynamically irrelevant in the same region of
parameters, i.e. at
N_{F}<N_{c}
and at
\mu_{\Phi}>m_{1}
or
\mu_{H}>\mu_{o}
at

\mu_{\Phi}<m_{1}
if
N_{F}>N_{c}
.

Moreover, some general properties of both theories such as
the
multiplicity of vacua with unbroken or broken flavor symmetry and the values of
vacuum condensates of corresponding chiral superfields
(i.e.
\langle{\overline{Q}}_{j}Q^{i}\rangle
and
\langle S\rangle
)
are the same in these
two theories, independently of whether the fields
\Phi
and
X
are irrelevant
or relevant
.

Nevertheless, once the fields
\Phi
and
X
become relevant (e.g. at

\mu_{\rm x}\ll\Lambda_{2}
), the phase states, the RG evolution, the mass spectra
etc.,
become very different in these two theories
. The properties of the

\Phi
- theory were described in detail above in the text. In general, if
X
is
sufficiently light and dynamically relevant, the dynamics of the softly broken

{\cal N}=2
SQCD becomes complicated and is described in Part III.

Finally, we trace now a transition to the slightly broken
{\cal N}=2
theory
with small
\mu_{\rm x}\ll\Lambda_{2}
and fixed
\Lambda_{2}
. For this, we write first
the appropriate form of the
effective superpotential obtained from (
39.19
),(
39.20
)

{\cal W}^{\rm eff}_{Q}={\overline{N}}_{c}S+\frac{m}{z_{Q}(\mu^{\rm pole}_{\rm x},\Lambda_{Q})}\,{\rm Tr}({\overline{Q}}Q)-\frac{1}{2z^{2}_{Q}(\mu^{\rm pole}_{\rm x},\Lambda_{Q})\mu_{\rm x}}\Biggl({\rm Tr}\,({\overline{Q}}Q)^{2}-\frac{1}{N_{c}}\Bigl({\rm Tr}\,{\overline{Q}}Q\Bigr)^{2}\Biggr),

\displaystyle S=\Biggl(\,\,\frac{\det{\overline{Q}}Q}{\Lambda_{Q}^{\rm{b_{o}}}}\,\,\Biggr)^{1/{\overline{N}}_{c}}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\Lambda_{Q}^{\rm{b_{o}}}=z^{N_{F}}_{Q}(\mu^{\rm pole}_{\rm x},\Lambda_{Q})\Lambda^{b_{2}}_{2}\mu^{N_{c}}_{\rm x}

(39.22)

and restore now the original normalization of the quark fields

\overline{\textbf{Q}},\textbf{Q}
appropriate for the slightly broken
{\cal N}=2

theory with varying
\mu_{\rm x}\ll\Lambda_{2}
and fixed
\Lambda_{2}
, see
(
39.18
),

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274pt{\cal W}^{\rm eff}_{Q}={\overline{N}}_{c}S+m\,{\rm Tr}(\overline{\textbf{Q}}\textbf{Q})-\frac{1}{2\mu_{\rm x}}\Biggl({\rm Tr}\,(\overline{\textbf{Q}}\textbf{Q})^{2}-\frac{1}{N_{c}}\Bigl({\rm Tr}\,\overline{\textbf{Q}}\textbf{Q}\Bigr)^{2}\Biggr),\hskip 11.74988ptS=\Bigl(\frac{\det\overline{\textbf{Q}}\textbf{Q}}{\Lambda^{{\rm b}_{2}}_{2}\mu^{N_{c}}_{\rm x}}\Bigr)^{1/{\overline{N}}_{c}}.

(39.23)

One can obtain now from (
39.23
) the values of the quark condensates

\langle\overline{\textbf{Q}}_{j}\textbf{Q}^{i}\rangle
at fixed
\Lambda_{2}
and small

\mu_{\rm x}\ll\Lambda_{2}
. Clearly, in comparison with values of
\langle{\overline{Q}}_{j}Q^{i}\rangle
in the direct
\Phi
-theory, the results for
\langle\overline{\textbf{Q}}_{j}\textbf{Q}^{i}\rangle
in (
39.23
) are obtained by the replacements :

m_{Q}\rightarrow m\,,\,\mu_{\Phi}\rightarrow\mu_{\rm x},\,\,\Lambda_{Q}^{\rm{b_{o}}}=\Lambda^{b_{2}}_{2}\mu^{N_{c}}_{\rm x}

in the effective superpotential, while multiplicities of vacua
are the same. From (
39.23
), the dependence of
\langle\overline{\textbf{Q}}_{j}\textbf{Q}^{i}\rangle
and
\langle S\rangle
on small

\mu_{\rm x}\ll\Lambda_{2}
at fixed
\Lambda_{2}
is trivial in all vacua,
\sim\mu_{\rm x}
.

It is worth only to recall that, as the effective
superpotential without the 4-quark term in the
{\cal N}=1
SQCD,
(
39.23
) is
not
a genuine low energy superpotential,
it can
be used only for finding the values of mean vacuum values

\langle{\overline{Q}}_{j}Q^{i}\rangle_{N_{c}}
and
\langle S\rangle_{N_{c}}
. The
genuine low energy superpotentials in each vacuum are given below in
Part III of the text.

With the above replacements, the expressions for
\langle{\overline{Q}}_{j}Q^{i}\rangle

in the region
\Lambda_{Q}\ll\mu_{\Phi}\ll m_{1}
for the direct
\Phi
-theory correspond here
to the hierarchy

m\ll\Lambda_{2}
, while those in the region
\mu_{\Phi}\gg m_{1}
correspond here to

m\gg\Lambda_{2}
. In the language of
[
22
]
used in
[
21
]
(see sections
6-9 therein), the correspondence between the
r
- vacua
[
22
,
21
]
of the
slightly broken
{\cal N}=2
theory with
0<\mu_{\rm x}/\Lambda_{2}\ll 1,\,\,0<m/\Lambda_{2}\ll 1
and our vacua in section 41 looks as

48
48

48

This correspondence is based on comparison of multiplicities of our vacua at

\mu_{\Phi}\ll m_{1}
described in section 41 and those of
r
- vacua at
m\ll\Lambda_{2}

and
\mu_{\rm x}\ll\Lambda_{2}
as these last are given in
[
21
]
.

:  a)
r=n_{1}
,  b)  our L - vacua with the unbroken or the L - type
ones with spontaneously broken flavor symmetry correspond, respectively, to the
first group of vacua of the non-baryonic branches with
r=0
and
r\geq 1,\,r\neq{\overline{N}}_{c}
in
[
21
]
,   c)  our S - vacua with the unbroken flavor
symmetry and
\rm br2
- vacua with the spontaneously broken flavor symmetry
correspond to the first type from the second group of vacua of the baryonic
branches with, respectively,
r=0
and
1\leq r<{\overline{N}}_{c}
in
[
