]
,   d)
our special vacua with
n_{1}={\overline{N}}_{c},\,n_{2}=N_{c}
correspond to the second type of
vacua from this group, see
[
21
]
.

Part III. Softly broken
\mathbf{\cal N}=2\rightarrow{\cal N}=1
SQCD.
Mass spectra in vacua with unbroken
\mathbf{Z_{2N_{c}-N_{F}}}
symmetry

40

Introduction

Considered are
{\cal N}=2\,\,SU(N_{c})
or
U(N_{c})
SQCD with

N_{F}<2N_{c}
equal mass quark flavors.
{\cal N}=2
supersymmetry is
softly broken down to
{\cal N}=1
by the mass term
\mu_{\rm x}{\rm Tr}\,(X^{2})
of colored adjoint scalar partners of gluons,
\mu_{\rm x}\ll\Lambda_{2}
(
\Lambda_{2}
is the scale factor of the
SU(N_{c})

gauge coupling).

There is a large number of different types of vacua in these theories
with both unbroken and spontaneously broken global flavor symmetry,

U(N_{F})\rightarrow U({\rm n}_{1})\times U({\rm n}_{2})
. We consider in
this paper the large subset of these vacua with the unbroken
non-trivial
Z_{2N_{c}-N_{F}\geq 2}
discrete symmetry, at different
hierarchies between the Lagrangian parameters
m\gtrless\Lambda_{2},\,\,\mu_{\rm x}\gtrless m
. The forms of low energy Lagrangians, quantum
numbers of light particles and mass spectra are described in the main
text for all these vacua.

The calculations of power corrections to the leading terms of the low
energy quark and dyon condensates are presented in two important
Appendices. The results agree with also presented in these Appendices

independent
calculations of these condensates using roots of
the   Seiberg-Witten spectral curve.
This agreement confirms in a
non-trivial way a self-consistency of the whole approach
.

Our results differ essentially from corresponding results in e.g.
related papers
[
40
]
,
[
41
]
and
[
48
]
of M.Shifman
and A.Yung (and in a number of their previous numerous papers on
this subject), and we explain in the text below the reasons for these
differences. (See also the extended critique of
a number of results of these authors in section 8.3 of
[
25
]
.

The masses and quantum numbers of BPS particles in

{\cal N}=2\,\,SU(2)
SYM and
SU(2)
SQCD with
N_{F}=1...4
quark
flavors at small
\mu_{\rm x}\neq 0
were found in seminal papers of N.Seiberg
and E.Witten
[
29
,
30
]
. They presented in particular the
corresponding spectral curves from which the masses and quantum
numbers of these BPS particles can be calculated at
\mu_{\rm x}\rightarrow 0
. The
forms of these curves have been generalized then, in particular, to

{\cal N}=2\,\,SU(N_{c})
SYM
[
31
]
and
{\cal N}=2\,\,SU(N_{c})

SQCD with
1\leq N_{F}\leq 2N_{c}
quark flavors
[
32
,
33
,
34
,
35
,
22
]
.
In what follows we deal mainly with
{\cal N}=2\,\,SU(N_{c})
SQCD
with
0<N_{F}<2N_{c}-1
flavors of equal mass quarks
Q^{i}_{a},\,{\overline{Q}}_{i}^{\,a},\,i=1...N_{F},\,\,a=1...N_{c}
. The Lagrangian of this UV free
theory, broken down to
{\cal N}=1
by the mass term
\mu_{\rm x}{\rm Tr}\,(X^{\rm adj}_{SU(N_{c})})^{2}
of adjoint scalars, can be written at
sufficiently high scale
\mu\gg\max\{\Lambda_{2},m\}
as (the gluon
exponents in Kahler terms are implied in (
40.1
) and
everywhere below)

K=\frac{2}{g^{2}(\mu)}{\rm Tr}\,\Bigl[(X^{\rm adj}_{SU(N_{c})})^{\dagger}X^{\rm adj}_{SU(N_{c})}\Bigr]_{N_{c}}+{\rm Tr}\,(Q^{\dagger}Q+{\overline{Q}}^{\,\dagger}{\overline{Q}})_{N_{c}}\,,\quad X^{\rm adj}_{SU(N_{c})}=T^{A}X^{A}\,,\quad A=1,\,...,N_{c}^{2}-1\,,

\displaystyle{\cal W}_{\rm matter}=\mu_{\rm x}{\rm Tr}\,(X^{\rm adj}_{SU(N_{c})})_{N_{c}}^{2}+{\rm Tr}\,\Bigl(m\,{\overline{Q}}Q-{\overline{Q}}\sqrt{2}X^{\rm adj}_{SU(N_{c})}Q\Bigr)_{N_{c}}\,,\quad{\rm Tr}\,(T^{A_{1}}T^{A_{2}})=\frac{1}{2}\,\delta^{A_{1}A_{2}}\,,

(40.1)

with the scale factor
\Lambda_{2}
of the gauge coupling
g(\mu)
, and
{\rm Tr}
in (
40.1
) is over all colors and flavors. The ”softly
broken”
{\cal N}=2
theory means
0<\mu_{\rm x}\ll\Lambda_{2}
. Considering
\mu_{\rm x}

and
m
as a soft background fields, there is the unbroken
U(1)

R-symmetry with the charges of fields and parameters:
r_{\lambda}=r_{\theta}=r_{Q}=r_{\overline{Q}}=1,\,r_{X}=r_{m}=r_{\Lambda_{2}}=0,\,r_{\mu_{\rm x}}=2
.

The spectral curve corresponding to (
40.1
) can be written at

N_{F}<2N_{c}-1
e.g. in the form
[
22
]

\displaystyle y^{2}=\prod_{i=1}^{N_{c}}(z+\phi_{i})^{2}-4\Lambda_{2}^{2N_{c}-N_{F}}(z+m)^{N_{F}}\,,\quad\sum_{i=1}^{N_{c}}\phi_{i}=0{\rm\,\,\,in\,\,\,SU(N_{c})}\,,

(40.2)

where
\{\phi_{i}\}
is a set of gauge invariant co-ordinates on the
moduli space. Note that, as it is, this curve can be used for the
calculation of
only BPS particle masses at
\mu_{\rm x}\rightarrow 0
only
.

In principle, it is possible to find at
\mu_{\rm x}\rightarrow 0
from the curve
(
40.2
) the quantum numbers and masses of all massive and
massless BPS particles in different multiple vacua of this theory.
But in   practice it is very difficult to do this for general values of

N_{c}
and
N_{F}
, especially as for quantum numbers . The important
property of the
SU(N_{c})
curve (
40.2
) which will be used in
the text below is that there are maximum
N_{c}-1
double roots.
And the   vacua we
will deal with below at
\mu_{\rm x}\neq 0
are just these vacua. In
other words,
in all
SU(N_{c})
vacua we will deal with, the
curve
(
40.2
)
has
N_{c}-1
double roots and two single roots
.

The first detailed attempt has been made in
[
22
]
(only the case

m=0
was considered) to classify the vacua of (
40.1
) for
general values of
N_{c}
and
N_{F}
, to find quantum numbers of light
BPS particles (massless at
\mu_{\rm x}\rightarrow 0
) in these vacua and forms of low
energy Lagrangians. It was found in particular that in all vacua the

SU(N_{c})
gauge symmetry is broken spontaneously at the scale

\mu\sim\Lambda_{2}
by higgsed adjoint scalars,
\langle X^{\rm adj}_{SU(N_{c})}\rangle\sim\Lambda_{2}
.

That this should happen can be understood qualitatively as follows,
see e.g.
[
26
]
. The perturbative NSVZ
\beta
-function of the
(effectively) massless unbroken
{\cal N}=2
SQCD is exactly one loop

[
12
,
13
]
. The theory (
40.1
) is UV free at

(2N_{c}-N_{F})>0
. At small
\mu_{\rm x}
and
m\ll\Lambda_{2}
, and if the whole matrix

\langle X^{adj}_{SU(N_{c})}\rangle\ll\Lambda_{2}
(i.e. in the effectively
massless at the scale
\mu\sim\Lambda_{2}
unbroken
\,{\cal N}=2
SQCD), the
coupling
g^{2}(\mu)
is well defined at
\mu\gg\Lambda_{2}
, has a pole at

\mu=\Lambda_{2}
and becomes negative at
\mu<\Lambda_{2}
. To avoid this unphysical
behavior,
the field
X
is necessarily higgsed breaking the

SU(N_{c})
group, with (at least some) components
\langle X^{A}\rangle\sim\Lambda_{2}
. (This becomes especially clear in the limit of
the unbroken
{\cal N}=2
theory at
\mu_{\rm x}\rightarrow 0
and small
m\ll\Lambda_{2}
,
when all chiral quark condensates and the gluino condensate, i.e.
their corresponding mean vacuum values
\langle...\rangle
, approach zero,
so   that all quarks are not higgsed in any case, and all particles are
very light at
\langle X^{\rm adj}_{SU(N_{c})}\rangle\ll\Lambda_{2}
).

Remind that exact values of
\langle X^{A}\rangle
account for possible
nonperturbative instanton contributions. And it is worth also to note
that, in the limit of unbroken
{\cal N}=2
SQCD with small
m
and

\mu_{\rm x}\rightarrow 0
, if there were no some components
\langle X^{A}\rangle\sim\Lambda_{2}
(i.e. all
\langle X^{A}\rangle\ll\Lambda_{2}
),
the
nonperturbative instanton contributions also would be non operative
at   the scale
\sim\Lambda_{2}
without both the corresponding fermion masses

\sim\Lambda_{2}
and the infrared cut off
\,\,\rho\lesssim\,1/\Lambda_{2}
supplied
by some
\langle X^{A}\rangle\sim\Lambda_{2}
. Therefore, the problem with

g^{2}(\mu<\Lambda_{2})<0
will survive in this case. Moreover, for the same
reasons, if there remains the non-Abelian subgroup unbroken at the
scale
\Lambda_{2}
,    it   has to be IR free (or at least conformal)
.

It was found in
[
22
]
that (at
m=0
) and
\mu_{\rm x}\rightarrow 0
there are
two qualitatively different branches of vacua. On the baryonic branch
the lower energy gauge group at
\mu<\Lambda_{2}
is
SU(N_{F}-N_{c})\times U^{2N_{c}-N_{F}}(1)
, while on non-baryonic branches these are
SU(n_{1})\times U^{N_{c}-n_{1}}(1),\,\,n_{1}\leq N_{F}/2
.

Besides, at
m\neq 0
and small
\mu_{\rm x}\neq 0
, the global flavor
symmetry
U(N_{F})
of (
40.1
) is unbroken or broken spontaneously
as
U(N_{F})\rightarrow U({\rm n}_{1})\times U({\rm n}_{2}),\,1\leq{\rm n}_{1}\leq[N_{f}/2]
in various
vacua
[
21
]
.

As was also first pointed out in
[
22
]
, there is the residual

Z_{2N_{c}-N_{F}}
discrete R-symmetry in theory (
40.1
).
This
symmetry will play a crucial role in what follows, as well as a
knowledge of multiplicities of all different types of vacua
, see e.g.

[
25
,
17
]
. The charges of   fields and
parameters in the superpotential of (
40.1
) under

Z_{2N_{c}-N_{F}}=\exp\{i\pi/(2N_{c}-N_{F})\}
transformation are:
q_{\lambda}=q_{\rm\theta}=1,\,\,q_{X}=q_{\rm m}=2,\,\,q_{Q}=q_{\,\overline{Q}}=q_{\Lambda_{2}}=0,\,\,q_{\mu_{\rm x}}=-2
. If this non-trivial at
2N_{c}-N_{F}\geq 2

discrete
Z_{2N_{c}-N_{F}}
symmetry is broken spontaneously in some vacua,
there will appear then the factor
2N_{c}-N_{F}
in their multiplicity.
Therefore, if multiplicities of different types of vacua are known, we
can see explicitly whether this
Z_{2N_{c}-N_{F}}
symmetry is broken
spontaneously or not in these vacua. As a result, it is seen the
following at
0<N_{F}<2N_{c}-1,\,m\ll\Lambda_{2}
(in the language of

[
25
,
17
]
).

I).  In
SU(N_{c})
theories at
m\ll\Lambda_{2}
(
\,\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1,2}\rangle_{N_{c}}
and
\langle S\rangle_{N_{c}}=\langle\lambda\lambda/32\pi^{2}\rangle
are the quark and gluino condensates
summed over all their colors ). The discrete
Z_{2N_{c}-N_{F}\geq 2}
symmetry is broken spontaneously.-

1) In L (large) vacua with the unbroken global flavor symmetry

U(N_{F})
, the multiplicity
2N_{c}-N_{F}
and with

\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)\rangle_{N_{c}}\sim\mu_{\rm x}\Lambda_{2},\,\,\langle S\rangle_{N_{c}}\sim\mu_{\rm x}\Lambda_{2}^{2},\,\,\langle{\rm Tr}(X^{adj}_{SU(N_{c})})^{2}\rangle\sim\Lambda_{2}^{2}
.

2) In Lt ( L-type ) vacua with the spontaneously broken flavor
symmetry,
U(N_{F})\rightarrow U({\rm n}_{1})\times U({\rm n}_{2}),\,\,1\leq{\rm n}_{1}\leq N_{F}/2
, the
multiplicity
(2N_{c}-N_{F})C^{\,{\rm n}_{1}}_{N_{F}},\,C^{\,{\rm n}_{1}}_{N_{F}}=(N_{F}!/{\rm n}_{1}!\,{\rm n}_{2}!)\,,

and   with
\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{N_{c}}\sim\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{N_{c}}\sim\mu_{\rm x}\Lambda_{2},\,\,\langle S\rangle_{N_{c}}\sim\mu_{\rm x}\Lambda_{2}^{2},\,\,\langle{\rm Tr}(X^{adj}_{SU(N_{c})})^{2}\rangle\sim\Lambda_{2}^{2}
.

3) In special vacua with
U(N_{F})\rightarrow U({\rm n}_{1}=N_{F}-N_{c})\times U({\rm n}_{2}=N_{c})
,
the multiplicity
(2N_{c}-N_{F})C^{\,N_{F}-N_{c}}_{N_{F}}
, and with
\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{N_{c}}\sim\mu_{\rm x}m,\,\,\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{N_{c}}=\mu_{\rm x}\Lambda_{2},\,\,\langle S\rangle_{N_{c}}\sim\mu_{\rm x}m\Lambda_{2},\,\,\langle{\rm Tr}(X^{adj}_{SU(N_{c})})^{2}\rangle\sim m\Lambda_{2}
.

While
Z_{2N_{c}-N_{F}\geq 2}
is unbroken. -

4) In br2 (br=breaking) vacua with
U(N_{F})\rightarrow U({\rm n}_{1})\times U({\rm n}_{2}),\,\,{\rm n}_{2}>N_{c}
, the multiplicity
(N_{F}-N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})C^{\,{\rm n}_{1}}_{N_{F}}
, and with
the dominant condensate
\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{N_{c}}\sim\mu_{\rm x}m\gg\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{N_{c}}\sim\mu_{\rm x}m(m/\Lambda_{2})^{\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{{\rm n}_{2}-N_{c}}}
,

\langle S\rangle_{N_{c}}\sim\mu_{\rm x}m^{2}(m/\Lambda_{2})^{(2N_{c}-N_{F})/({\rm n}_{2}-N_{c})},\,\,\langle{\rm Tr}(X^{adj}_{SU(N_{c})})^{2}\rangle\sim m^{2}
.

5) In S (small) vacua with unbroken
U(N_{F})
, the multiplicity

N_{F}-N_{c}
and with
\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)\rangle_{N_{c}}\sim\mu_{\rm x}m,\,\,\langle S\rangle_{N_{c}}\sim\mu_{\rm x}m^{2}(m/\Lambda_{2})^{(2N_{c}-N_{F})/(N_{F}-N_{c})},\,\,\langle{\rm Tr}(X^{adj}_{SU(N_{c})})^{2}\rangle\sim m^{2}
(see

[
25
,
17
]
for all details).

II).
Z_{2N_{c}-N_{F}\geq 2}
is unbroken in all multiple vacua of
SU(N_{c})

theories at
m\gg\Lambda_{2}
(see Part III.2 in the table of contents).

III). There are additional vs (very special) vacua with e.g.

N_{c}+1\leq N_{F}\leq 2N_{c}-1
, with the spontaneously broken global flavor
symmetry
U(N_{F})\rightarrow U({\rm n}_{1}=N_{F}-N_{c})\times U({\rm n}_{2}=N_{c}),\,\,\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{N_{c}}=\mu_{\rm x}m,\,\,\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{N_{c}}=0,\,\,\,\langle S\rangle_{N_{c}}=0,\,\,\langle{\rm Tr}(X^{adj}_{SU(N_{c})})^{2}\rangle=0
, and the multiplicity

C^{N_{F}-N_{c}}_{N_{F}}
in
U(N_{c})
theories at all
m\gtrless\Lambda_{2}
(such
vacua are absent in
SU(N_{c})
theories).

Z_{2N_{c}-N_{F}\geq 2}
is unbroken in these vs-vacua at all
m\gtrless\Lambda_{2}
.

It was stated in
[
22
]
that
in all vacua
of
SU(N_{c})

(
40.1
),   baryonic and non-baryonic, all light particles in low
energy Lagrangians with
m=0
and small
\mu_{\rm x}
, i.e. quarks, gluons
and scalars in non-Abelian sectors and corresponding particles in
Abelian ones, are pure magnetic.

Really, the final purpose of
[
22
]
was an attempt to derive the
Seiberg duality for
{\cal N}=1
SQCD
[
5
,
2
]
. The idea was as
follows. On the one hand, the electric
{\cal N}=2
SQCD in
(
40.1
) with small both
\mu_{\rm x}
and
m
flows clearly to the
standard
{\cal N}=1
electric SQCD with
SU(N_{c})
gauge group,
N_{F}

flavors of light electric quarks and without adjoint scalars

X^{\rm adj}
at
m={\rm const},\,\,\mu_{\rm x}\rightarrow\infty
and fixed

\Lambda_{Q}^{3N_{c}-N_{F}}=\Lambda_{2}^{2N_{c}-N_{F}}\mu_{\rm x}^{N_{c}}
. On the other hand, the
authors of
[
22
]
expected that, starting in (
40.1
) at

\mu_{\rm x}\ll\Lambda_{2}
from vacua of the baryonic branch with the
SU({\overline{N}}_{c}=N_{F}-N)

low energy   gauge group
by itself
and increasing then

\mu_{\rm x}\gg\Lambda_{2}
, they will obtain Seiberg’s dual
{\cal N}=1
pure magnetic
SQCD with
SU({\overline{N}}_{c}=N_{F}-N_{c})
gauge group and
N_{F}
flavors of massless
dual magnetic quarks. (But, in any case,
N^{2}_{F}
light Seiberg’s
mesons
M^{i}_{j}\rightarrow({\overline{Q}}_{j}Q^{i})
of dual
{\cal N}=1\,\,SU({\overline{N}}_{c})

SQCD
[
5
]
were missing in this approach, see section 8 in

[
25
]
for a more detailed critique of
[
22
]
).

The results from
[
22
]
were generalized then in
[
21
]
to
nonzero mass quarks, with emphasis on properties of flavor symmetry
breaking in different vacua. Besides, multiplicities of various vacua
were discussed in this paper in some details. (A complete detailed
description of different vacua multiplicities and values of quark and
gluino condensates,
\langle{\overline{Q}}_{j}Q^{i}\rangle_{N_{c}}
and
\langle S\rangle_{N_{c}}
, in various vacua and at different hierarchies between

\mu_{\rm x},\,m
and
\Lambda_{2}
(or
\Lambda_{Q}^{3N_{c}-N_{F}}=\Lambda_{2}^{2N_{c}-N_{F}}\mu_{\rm x}^{N_{c}}
) see in
[
25
,
26
,
17
]
).

But further detailed investigations of light particles quantum numbers in theory (
40.1
), in some simplest examples with small values
of
N_{c}
and
N_{F}
, did not confirm the statement
[
22
]
that all
these particles, and in particular light quarks, are pure magnetic.

Specifically, the
SU(N_{c}=3)
vacua of (
40.1
), with
N_{F}=4,\,\,{\rm n}_{1}=2,\,{\rm n}_{2}=N_{F}-{\rm n}_{1}=2
, (these are Lt - vacua with the gluino condensate

\langle S\rangle_{N_{c}}\sim\mu_{\rm x}\Lambda_{2}^{2}

and spontaneously broken non-trivial
Z_{2N_{c}-N_{F}=2}
symmetry at

m\ll\Lambda_{2}
), and vacua with
N_{F}=5,\,\,{\rm n}_{1}=2,\,{\rm n}_{2}=3
(these
are special vacua with
\langle S\rangle_{N_{c}}\sim m\,\mu_{\rm x}\Lambda_{2}
,

Z_{2N_{c}-N_{F}=1}
symmetry acts trivially in this case and gives   no
restrictions on the form of
\langle X^{\rm adj}_{SU(3)}\rangle
),
were considered in details at
\mu_{\rm x}\rightarrow 0
and
m\lessgtr\Lambda_{2}
in

[
36
]
. Besides, the vs (very special)
U(N_{c}=3)=SU(N_{c}=3)\times U^{(0)}(1)
vacua with
N_{F}=5,\,\,{\rm n}_{1}=2,\,{\rm n}_{2}=3,\,\langle S\rangle_{N_{c}}=0
were considered earlier in
[
37
]
.
In these vacua, the light charged particles are not magnetic
monopoles, but dyons
D^{\,i}_{a},\,{\overline{D}}^{\,a}_{i},\,\,a=1,\,2,\,\,i=1...4(5)
(massless at
\mu_{\rm x}\rightarrow 0
) which form
SU(2)
doublet
of dyons with the
SU(3)
fundamental electric charges and with
the hybrid magnetic charges which are fundamental with respect
to
SU(2)
but adjoint with respect to
SU(3)
.

We can propose e.g. the following picture for these UV free

SU(N_{c}=3)
theories with
N_{F}=4
or
N_{F}=5
at
m\ll\Lambda_{2},\,\mu_{\rm x}\rightarrow 0
. To avoid
g^{2}(\mu<\Lambda_{2})<0
and without restrictions from the
non-trivial unbroken
Z_{2N_{c}-N_{F}}
symmetry, the electric gauge
group
SU(3)
is   higgsed in these vacua by
\langle X^{\rm adj}_{SU(3)}\rangle\sim diag(\Lambda_{2},\Lambda_{2},-2\Lambda_{2})
, i.e.
SU(3)\rightarrow SU(2)\times U(1)
, so that all original pure electrically charged
quarks acquire masses
\sim\Lambda_{2}
. But, due to strong coupling

a(\mu\sim\Lambda_{2})=N_{c}g^{2}(\mu\sim\Lambda_{2})/8\pi^{2}\sim 1
and very specific
properties of the enhanced
{\cal N}=2
SUSY, the heavy flavored
electric quarks
Q^{\,i}_{a},\,{\overline{Q}}^{\,a}_{i},\,\,a=1,\,2,\,\,i=1...4(5)
(i.e.
SU(2)
color doublet) with equal masses
\sim\Lambda_{2}
combined
with two heavy unflavored pure magnetic monopoles with the same equal
masses
\sim\Lambda_{2}
and with above described hybrid magnetic charges, and
formed the
SU(2)
doublet of light flavored BPS dyons

D^{\,i}_{a},\,{\overline{D}}^{\,a}_{i}
, massless at
\mu_{\rm x}\rightarrow 0
.

These massless at
\mu_{\rm x}\rightarrow 0
quark-like dyons
D^{\,i}_{a},\,{\overline{D}}^{\,a}_{i},\,\,a,i=1,\,2
coupled then with massless
SU(2)
adjoint gluons and
scalars (which transformed from pure electric to dyonic ones, and this
is a most surprising phenomenon) and formed the dyonic
{\cal N}=2\,SU(2)
non-Abelian group (conformal at
N_{F}=4
and IR free at

N_{F}=5
at lower energies
\mu<\Lambda_{2}
.

As follows from the curve (
40.2
) and
from
[
44
]
, the
SU(2)
dyons are higgsed
simultaneously at small
\mu_{\rm x}\neq 0
e.g. as
\langle{\overline{D}}^{\,a}_{i}\rangle=\langle D_{\,a}^{i}\rangle\sim\delta^{i}_{a}(\mu_{\rm x}\Lambda_{2})^{1/2},\,a,i=1,2
(and for this to be possible in the ground
state they have to be massless at
\mu_{\rm x}\rightarrow 0
and mutually local). As a
result, the whole color
SU(2)
group is broken and
({\rm n}_{1}=2)^{2}=4
long

{\cal N}=2
multiplets of massive gluons with masses
\sim g_{D}(\mu_{\rm x}\Lambda_{2})^{1/2}
are formed. Besides, the global flavor
U(N_{F})
symmetry
is broken spontaneously as
U(N_{F})\rightarrow U({\rm n}_{1}=2)\times U(N_{F}-2)
at

\mu_{\rm x}\neq 0,\,\,\delta_{ij}=(m_{i}-m_{j})=0
, and
massless
{\cal N}=1
Nambu-Goldstone multiplets
appear (in essence, these are not higgsed dyons).

These higgsed
SU(2)
quark-like dyons
D^{\,i}_{a},{\overline{D}}^{\,a}_{i},\,i,a=1,2

are mutually non-local with all heavy original quarks with
SU(3)

colors and with the
SU(2)
doublet of pure magnetic monopoles, all
with masses
\sim\Lambda_{2}
. Therefore, when these dyons
D^{\,i}_{a},{\overline{D}}^{a}_{i}
are higgsed at small
\mu_{\rm x}\neq 0
as
\langle D^{\,i}_{a}\rangle=\langle{\overline{D}}_{i}^{\,a}\rangle\sim\delta^{i}_{a}\,(\mu_{\rm x}\Lambda_{2})^{1/2}
, this results in a
weak confinement of these heavy charged particles, the string tension
is
\sigma^{1/2}\sim(\mu_{\rm x}\Lambda_{2})^{1/2}\ll\Lambda_{2}
. (The confinement is weak
in a sense that the string tension is much smaller than particle
masses). These heavy particles form hadrons with masses
\sim\Lambda_{2}
.
In the case of
U(N_{c}=3)
vs-vacua with
N_{F}=5
and
\langle S\rangle_{N_{c}}=0
from
[
37
]
, there are in addition the
massless at
\mu_{\rm x}\rightarrow 0

SU(2)
singlet BPS dyons
D_{3},{\overline{D}}_{3}
with
\langle{\overline{D}}_{3}\rangle=\langle D_{3}\rangle\sim(\mu_{\rm x}\Lambda_{2})^{1/2}
at
\mu_{\rm x}\neq 0
. Now the additional long
{\cal N}=2\,\,U(1)
multiplet of massive gluon with the mass
\sim g_{D}(\mu_{\rm x}\Lambda_{2})^{1/2}
is formed.

Besides, it was shown explicitly in
[
37
,
36
]
at
\mu_{\rm x}\rightarrow 0
on
examples considered, how vacua with some massless at
m\gg\Lambda_{2}
and

\mu_{\rm x}\rightarrow 0
pure electric original quarks
Q^{i}
can evolve at
m\ll\Lambda_{2}

into vacua   with only massless dyons
D^{i}
. E.g., in the vicinity of
the Argyres-Douglas point
m=m_{0}\sim\Lambda_{2}
, there are vacua in which

additional
corresponding particles become light. At
m=m_{0}
the
vacua   collide, all these particles become massless, and the lower energy
regime at
\mu<\Lambda_{2}
is conformal. In particular, both some flavored
pure electric quarks
Q^{i}
and flavored dyonic quarks
D^{i}
are
massless at this point (as well as some pure magnetic monopoles). The
masses of these quarks with
fixed quantum numbers
behave
continuously but non-analytically in
m/\Lambda_{2}
at this special point
m=m_{0}
.
E.g., in selected at
m\lessgtr m_{0}
vacua colliding at
m=m_{0}

with other vacua, the mass of some light pure electric quarks
Q^{i}

stays at zero at
m\geq m_{0}
, then begins to grow at
m<m_{0}
and
becomes
\sim\Lambda_{2}
at
m\ll\Lambda_{2}
, while the mass of light composite
dyonic quarks
D^{i}
is zero in these vacua at
m\leq m_{0}
, then begins
to grow at
m>m_{0}
and becomes large at
m\gg m_{0}
.
Equivalently
, tracing e.g. the evolution with
m/\Lambda_{2}
in the small
vicinity of
m=m_{0}
of
quantum numbers
of, separately, massless
or nearly massless flavored quark-like particles at
m\lessgtr m_{0}
,
one can say that their quantum numbers jump at the collision point

m=m_{0}
. I.e.: a) the massless particles at
m>m_{0}
are pure
electric quarks
Q^{i}
, while they are dyonic quarks
D^{i}
at
m<m_{0}
; b) the very light (but not massless) flavored quark-like
particles are the dyonic quarks
D^{i}
at
m>m_{0}
, while they are
pure electric quarks
Q^{i}
at
m<m_{0}
. These two views of the
non-analytic behavior at the collision point
m=m_{0}
when traversing
the small vicinity of the collision point, i.e. either looking at the
non-analytic but continuous evolution of small masses of particles
with fixed quantum numbers, or looking on the jumps of quantum numbers
of, separately, massless particles and of nearly massless particles,
are clearly
equivalent and indistinguishable
because all these
particles simultaneously become massless at the collision point

m=m_{0}
. These are two different projections (or two sides) of the
one phenomenon.

The examples of these conformal regimes for the
{\cal N}=2\,\,SU(2)

theory were described earlier e.g. in
[
38
,
39
]
. But it is clear
beforehand that to avoid unphysical
g^{2}(\mu<\Lambda_{2})<0
in UV free

{\cal N}=2\,\,SU(2)
with
N_{F}=1,2,3
and
m\ll\Lambda_{2}
, the whole gauge group
is higgsed necessarily at the scale
\mu\sim\Lambda_{2}
by
\langle X^{\rm adj}_{SU(2)}\rangle\sim\Lambda_{2},\,SU(2)\rightarrow U(1)

[
29
,
30
]
, so that
all original pure electrically charged particles are indeed heavy,
with masses
\sim\Lambda_{2}
.

The properties of light particles with masses
\ll\Lambda_{2}
in theory
(
40.1

U(N_{c})

U^{(0)}(1)

(this is
forbidden in these vacua by the unbroken non-trivial

). But they changed later their mind to the
opposite

49
49

SU(N_{c})

and, extrapolating freely by analogy their previous results for

that this

have received large masses

independently of whether these terms originated directly from

\langle X^{adj}_{SU(N_{c})}\rangle
or from unrecognized ”outside”.

The authors of
[
22
]
understood that, at
m\ll\Lambda_{2}
and small

\mu_{\rm x}
, the quarks
Q^{i}
from

) pure

, when evolved to
m\ll\Lambda_{2}
, have only
light quarks with nonzero pure magnetic charges. And they considered
this as a literal evidence that phases with light pure electrically and
pure magnetically charged quarks are not different and can transform
freely into each other by some unidentified mechanism. And they
supposed that in vacua of the baryonic branch
just this
mysterious mechanism transformed at
m\ll\Lambda_{2}
all light pure electric
particles from
SU({\overline{N}}_{c})
into light pure magnetic ones
.

Remind that, as described above on the examples of vacua with
spontaneously broken or trivial
Z_{2N_{c}-N_{F}}
symmetry, even in such
vacua at
m\ll\Lambda_{2}
the electric quarks do not literally transform by
themselves into light dyonic ones. The mechanism is different, see
above. Therefore, we will consider in what follows that such
mysterious mechanism which transforms literally at
m\ll\Lambda_{2}
light
electric quarks
in vacua with unbroken non-trivial

Z_{2N_{c}-N_{F}\geq 2}
symmetry
into light magnetic (or dyonic) ones
does not exist really.

50

50

It is clear from
[
40
,
41
]
that these authors also disagree with
pure magnetic

quarks from
[

]
.

Our purpose in this paper is similar to
[
40
,
41
]
, but we limit
ourselves at
m\ll\Lambda_{2}
only to vacua of baryonic branch with the
non-trivial unbroken discrete
Z_{2N_{c}-N_{F}\geq 2}
symmetry. As will be
seen from the text below, this
Z_{2N_{c}-N_{F}}
symmetry is strong enough
and   helps greatly.

We introduce also the two following assumptions of general
character. -

Assumption A:
in unbroken
{\cal N}=2
(i.e. at
\mu_{\rm x}\rightarrow 0
)
and at least in considered vacua with the non-trivial unbroken

Z_{2N_{c}-N_{F}\geq 2}
symmetry, the original pure electrically charged
particles receive additional contributions to their masses only from
higgsed

\langle X\rangle\neq 0
(remind that
\langle X\rangle

includes in general all nonperturbative instanton contributions). In
other words, at
\mu_{\rm x}\rightarrow 0

they are BPS particles
in vacua
considered. For instance, if light quarks
Q^{i}
with
m\ll\Lambda_{2}
do not
receive contributions to their masses from some
\langle X^{A}\rangle\neq 0
, they remain as they were, i.e. the light electric
quarks with masses
m
. The opposite is not true in this very special

{\cal N}=2
theory in the strong coupling (and nonperturbative)
regime
a(\mu\sim\Lambda_{2})=N_{c}g^{2}(\mu\sim\Lambda_{2})/8\pi^{2}=O(1)
. The original
pure electric quarks which have received masses
\sim\Lambda_{2}
from some

\langle X^{A}\rangle\sim\Lambda_{2}
, can combine e.g. with some heavy
composite pure magnetic monopoles, also with masses
\sim\Lambda_{2}
, and
form massless at
\mu_{\rm x}\rightarrow 0
composite BPS dyons. And all that.

This assumption A about BPS properties of original electric
particles is checked in the text below by two
independent calculations of quark and gluino condensates
.

Assumption B:
there are no extra massless particles (i.e. in addition
to standard Nambu-Goldstone particles due to spontaneous breaking of global

U(N_{F})\rightarrow U){\rm n}_{1})\times U({\rm n}_{2})
at
m_{i}=m
and small
\mu_{\rm x}\neq 0
) in
considered vacua of the
SU(N_{c})
theory
(
40.1
). This more particular
assumption will help us to clarify the quantum numbers of
(2N_{c}-N_{F})

massless at
\mu_{\rm x}\rightarrow 0
charged BPS dyons
D_{j}
, see section 41.1.

And, as a result,
there are no massless particles at all in
considered vacua of
SU(N_{c})
theories at
\mu_{\rm x}\neq 0,\,\,m_{i}\neq 0,\,\,(\delta m)_{ij}\neq 0
.

Besides, to calculate mass spectra in some
{\cal N}=1
SQCD theories
obtained from
{\cal N}=2
after increasing masses
\sim\mu_{\rm x}
of adjoint
scalar multiplets
X^{\rm adj}
and decoupling them as heavy ones at scales

\mu<\mu_{\rm x}^{\rm pole}
, we use the dynamical scenario introduced in

[
20
]
.
But it is really needed in this paper only for those
few cases with
3N_{c}/2<N_{F}<2N_{c}-1
when so obtained
{\cal N}=1

SQCD enters at lower energies
\mu<\Lambda_{Q}
the strong coupling
conformal regime with

a_{*}=O(1)
, see section 41.4 below.

Remind that this scenario
[
20
]
assumes for these cases that at
those even lower scales where this conformal regime is broken
when quarks are either higgsed or decouple as heavy,

no additional parametrically light
composite solitons are formed at these scales in this
{\cal N}=1

SQCD
without elementary colored scalar fields
X^{\rm adj}
.
(‘Additional’ means here in addition to the standards
Nambu-Golstone particles).

51
51

51

It is worth noting that the appearance of additional light solitons
will influence the ’t Hooft triangles of this
{\cal N}=1
theory.

Let us emphasize that
this dynamical scenario satisfies all
those checks of Seiberg’s duality hypothesis for
{\cal N}=1

SQCD which were used in
[
5
]
, see e.g.

[
11
,
18
,
17
,
25
,

. And it allows to calculate mass
spectra of the standard
{\cal N}=1
SQCD and SQCD-type
.theories. And it was shown in these papers that the mass spectra
of the direct and Seiberg’s dual theories are parametrically different.

And this shows that
all those checks of
{\cal N}=1
duality
from
[
4
,
5
]
, although necessary, may well be insufficient
.

Besides, to the best of our knowledge,
the results obtained in the framework of
this scenario look self-consistent, satisfy a large number of
independent checks, and do not contradict
to any proven results.

In other cases, if   this
{\cal N}=1
SQCD stays in
the IR free weak coupling logarithmic
regime, its dynamics is simple and clear and there is no need for
any additional dynamical assumptions.

|A|\ll|B|
.

\displaystyle\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{N_{c}}=\mu_{\rm x}\,{\hat{m}}+\frac{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}{{\rm n}_{2}-N_{c}}\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{N_{c}}\approx\hat{m}\,\mu_{\rm x},\,\,\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{N_{c}}\approx\mu_{\rm x}\hat{m}\Bigl(\frac{\hat{m}}{\Lambda_{2}}\Bigr)^{\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{{\rm n}_{2}-N_{c}}},\,\,

(41.1)

\frac{\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{N_{c}}}{\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{N_{c}}}\approx\Bigl(\frac{\hat{m}}{\Lambda_{2}}\Bigr)^{\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{{\rm n}_{2}-N_{c}}}\ll 1\,,\,\,N_{c}+1<N_{F}<2N_{c}-1\,,\,\,\hat{m}=\frac{N_{c}}{N_{c}-n_{2}}\,m\,,\,\,{\overline{N}}_{c}\equiv N_{F}-N_{c},

\langle S\rangle_{N_{c}}=\frac{\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{N_{c}}\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{N_{c}}}{\mu_{\rm x}}\approx\mu_{\rm x}\,\hat{m}^{2}\Bigl(\frac{\hat{m}}{\Lambda_{2}}\Bigr)^{\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{{\rm n}_{2}-N_{c}}}\ll\mu_{\rm x}m^{2},\hskip 11.74988pt{\rm n}_{2}>N_{c}\,,

and this shows that
the multiplicity of these vacua
is
N_{\rm br2}=({\rm n}_{2}-N_{c})\,C^{\,{\rm n}_{2}}_{N_{F}}=({\overline{N}}_{c}-n_{1})\,C^{\,{\rm n}_{1}}_{N_{F}}
, the
factor
C^{\,{\rm n}_{1}}_{N_{F}}=N_{F}!/({\rm n}_{1}!\,{\rm n}_{2}!)
originates from the
spontaneous breaking
U(N_{F})\rightarrow U({\rm n}_{1})\times U({\rm n}_{2})
of the global
flavor symmetry of (
40.1
).

Because the multiplicity
N_{\rm br2}
of these br2 vacua in
(
41.1
) does not contain the factor
2N_{c}-N_{F}
, this shows that the
non-trivial at
2N_{c}-N_{F}\geq 2
discrete symmetry
Z_{2N_{c}-N_{F}}

is not
broken spontaneously in these vacua
.

41.1

SU(N_{c})
, smallest
\mu_{\rm x}

We describe first the overall qualitative picture of
various stages (in order of decreasing energy scale) of the gauge and
flavor symmetry breaking in these br2 vacua. As will be shown below,
it leads in a practically unique way to a right multiplicity of these
vacua with unbroken discrete
Z_{2N_{c}-N_{F}\geq 2}
symmetry, and it is
non-trivial to achieve this. The value of
\mu_{\rm x}
is taken in this
section to be very small,
0<\mu_{\rm x}<\langle\Lambda^{SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle^{2}/\Lambda_{2}
, see (
41.1.4
) below.

1)  As was explained in Introduction, at small
m
and
\mu_{\rm x}
,
the adjoint field
X^{\rm adj}_{SU(N_{c})}
higgses necessarily the UV free
SU(N_{c})
group at the
largest scale
\mu\sim\Lambda_{2}
to avoid
g^{2}(\mu<\Lambda_{2})<0
. In the case
considered this first stage looks as:
\,SU(N_{c})\rightarrow SU(N_{F}-N_{c})\times U^{(1)}(1)\times U(1)^{2N_{c}-N_{F}-1}

[
22
]
, while all other components
of
\langle X^{A}\rangle\lesssim m
are much smaller, see below
(
41.1.3
),(
41.1.6
),

\displaystyle\langle\sqrt{2}\,X^{\rm adj}_{SU(2N_{c}-N_{F})}\,\rangle=C_{2N_{c}-N_{F}}\Lambda_{2}\,{\rm diag}(\,\underbrace{\,0}_{{\overline{N}}_{c}};\underbrace{\,\omega^{0},\,\,\omega^{1},\,...,\omega^{2N_{c}-N_{F}-1}}_{2N_{c}-N_{F}}\,)\,,\,\,\omega=\exp\{\frac{2\pi i}{2N_{c}-N_{F}}\},\,\,\,\,\,

(41.1.1)

\sqrt{2}\,X^{(1)}_{U(1)}=a_{1}\,{\rm diag}(\,\underbrace{\,1}_{{\overline{N}}_{c}}\,;\,\underbrace{\,c_{1}}_{2N_{c}-N_{F}}\,),\hskip 11.74988ptc_{1}=-\,\frac{{\overline{N}}_{c}}{2N_{c}-N_{F}}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\langle a_{1}\rangle=\frac{1}{c_{1}}\,m\,,\hskip 11.74988ptC_{2N_{c}-N_{F}}=O(1)\,.

This pattern of symmetry breaking is required by the unbroken

Z_{2N_{c}-N_{F}\geq 2}
discrete symmetry. Remind that charges under

Z_{2N_{c}-N_{F}}=\exp\{\pi i/(2N_{c}-N_{F})\}
transformation are:

q_{\lambda}=q_{\theta}=1,\,\,q_{X}=q_{\rm m}=2,\,\,q_{Q}=q_{\overline{Q}}=q_{\Lambda_{2}}=0,\,\,\mu_{\rm x}=-2
, so that
\langle a_{1}\rangle
respects

Z_{2N_{c}-N_{F}}
symmetry, while the form of
\langle\sqrt{2}\,X^{\rm adj}_{SU(2N_{c}-N_{F})}\,\rangle
ensures the right behavior under
Z_{2N_{c}-N_{F}}

transformation up to interchanging terms in (
41.1.1
)
(the Weyl symmetry).

As a result, all original
SU(2N_{c}-N_{F})
charged electric particles
acquire large masses
\sim\Lambda_{2}
and decouple at lower energies

\mu<\Lambda_{2}/(\rm several)
. But due to strong coupling,

a(\mu\sim\Lambda_{2})=N_{c}g^{2}(\mu\sim\Lambda_{2})/2\pi\sim 1
and the enhanced

{\cal N}=2
SUSY, the light composite BPS dyons
D_{j},{\overline{D}}_{j},\,\,j=1...2N_{c}-N_{F}
(massless at
\mu_{\rm x}\rightarrow 0
) are formed in this sector at
the scale
\mu\sim\Lambda_{2}
, their number is required by the unbroken

Z_{2N_{c}-N_{F}\geq 2}
symmetry which operates interchanging them among one
another. This is seen also from the spectral curve (
40.2
): it
has
2N_{c}-N_{F}
unequal double roots
e_{j}\approx\omega^{\,j-1}\Lambda_{2},\,\,j=1...2N_{c}-N_{F},
corresponding to these
2N_{c}-N_{F}
massless BPS solitons

[
22
,
21
]
. The charges of these dyons
D_{j}
are discussed below
in detail. They are all flavor singlets, their
U^{(1)}(1)
and

SU(N_{c})
baryon charges which are
SU(2N_{c}-N_{F})
singlets are pure
electric, while they are coupled with the whole set of
U^{2N_{c}-N_{F}-1}(1)

independent light Abelian (massless at
\mu_{\rm x}\rightarrow 0
) multiplets in
(
41.1.2
).

All original electric particles with
SU({\overline{N}}_{c})
colors remain
light
, i.e. with masses
\ll\Lambda_{2}
. This is a consequence of the
non-trivial unbroken
Z_{2N_{c}-N_{F}\geq 2}
symmetry and their BPS
properties. As for quarks with
SU({\overline{N}}_{c})
colors, the appearance in
the   superpotential of the mass terms like
\sim\Lambda_{2}{\rm Tr\,}({\overline{Q}}C_{Q}Q)_{{\overline{N}}_{c}}
with the constants
C^{i}_{Q}
such that this term will be

Z_{2N_{c}-N_{F}\geq 2}
invariant is impossible, independently of the source
of such terms. Therefore, the unbroken
Z_{2N_{c}-N_{F}\geq 2}
is sufficient
by itself to forbid
SU({\overline{N}}_{c})
quark masses
\sim\Lambda_{2}
. Similarly, it
also forbids (as well as the unbroken
U(1)
R-symmetry) the
”outside”contributions in the superpotential of mass terms of adjoint
scalars  like
\Lambda_{2}(X^{A}_{SU(N_{c})})^{2}
or
m(X^{A}_{SU(N_{c})})^{2}
.

But we will need below somewhat stronger assumption
{\bf"A"}

formulated in Introduction that all
SU(N_{c})
quarks are BPS
particles, i.e. they receive additional contributions to their masses

only directly
from the couplings with
\langle X^{(adj)}_{SU(N_{c})}\rangle
, see below. This forbids also the
appearance of
any ”outside”
(i.e. originating not from the
direct couplings with
\langle X^{(adj)}_{SU(N_{c})}\rangle
)
contributions to their masses, e.g. the additional contributions in
the superpotential
c_{m}m{\rm Tr\,}({\overline{Q}}Q)_{{\overline{N}}_{c}},\,c_{m}=O(1)
,
although these are not forbidden by the unbroken
Z_{2N_{c}-N_{F}\geq 2}
.
Besides, this BPS assumption
{\bf"A"}
concerns all original
electric particles of
SU(N_{c})
. On the other hand, it is natural to
expect that all original particles of the
SU(N_{c})
theory have the
same properties, in the sense that in considered isolated vacua at

\mu_{\rm x}\rightarrow 0
either they all are BPS or they all are not BPS. And
because the ”outside”  contributions
\sim\Lambda_{2}
to masses in the
superpotential are forbiden for
SU({\overline{N}}_{c})
quarks and scalars, and

\sim m
are forbidden for scalars, then all ”outside”  contributions   to
masses are forbidden for all original electric particles, i.e. they   all
are indeed the BPS particles. (The absence of such additional
contributions is confirmed by
two independent
calculations of

SU({\overline{N}}_{c})
quark condensates, see (
41.1.14
) and the text just
below it).

The
SU({\overline{N}}_{c})
gauge symmetry can still be considered as unbroken at
scales
m\ll\mu<\Lambda_{2}
(it is broken only at the scale
\sim m
due to
higgsed
X^{\rm adj}_{SU({\overline{N}}_{c})}
, see below). Therefore, after
integrating out all heavy particles with masses
\sim\Lambda_{2}
, the general
form of the superpotential at the scale
\mu_{\rm cut}=\Lambda_{2}/{(\rm several)}
can be written as (the dots in (
41.1.2
) denote
smaller power corrections)

\displaystyle\widehat{{\mathcal{W}}}_{\rm matter}={\mathcal{W}}_{SU({\overline{N}}_{c})}+{\mathcal{W}}_{D}+{\mathcal{W}}_{a_{1}}+\dots\,,

(41.1.2)

{\mathcal{W}}_{SU({\overline{N}}_{c})}={\rm Tr}\,\Bigl[{\overline{Q}}\Bigl(m-a_{1}-\sqrt{2}X^{\rm adj}_{SU({\overline{N}}_{c})}\Bigr)Q\Bigr]_{{\overline{N}}_{c}}+\widetilde{\mu}_{\rm x}{\rm Tr}\,(X^{\rm adj}_{SU({\overline{N}}_{c})})^{2}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\widetilde{\mu}_{\rm x}=\mu_{\rm x}(1+\delta_{2})\,,

{\mathcal{W}}_{D}=(m-c_{1}a_{1})\sum_{j=1}^{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{\overline{D}}_{j}D_{j}-\sum_{j=1}^{2N_{c}-N_{F}}a_{D,j}{\overline{D}}_{j}D_{j}-\mu_{\rm x}\Lambda_{2}\sum_{j=1}^{2N_{c}-N_{F}}\omega^{j-1}a_{D,j}+

+\mu_{\rm x}L\sum_{j=1}^{2N_{c}-N_{F}}a_{D,j}+O\Bigl(\mu_{\rm x}a_{D}^{2}\Bigr)\,,

{\mathcal{W}}_{a_{1}}=\frac{\mu_{\rm x}}{2}(1+\delta_{1})\frac{{\overline{N}}_{c}N_{c}}{2N_{c}-N_{F}}\,a^{2}_{1}+\mu_{\rm x}N_{c}\delta_{3}\,a_{1}(m-c_{1}a_{1})+\mu_{\rm x}N_{c}\delta_{4}(m-c_{1}a_{1})^{2}\,,

where
a_{D,j}
are the light neutral scalars,
L
is the Lagrange
multiplier,
\langle L\rangle=O(m)
, while all
\delta_{i}=O(1)
.

These terms with
\delta_{i}
in the superpotential (
41.1.2
)
originate finally from
the combined effect
of : a) the quantum
loops with heavy particles with masses
\sim\Lambda_{2}
, integrated over the
energy range
[\mu_{\rm cut}=\Lambda_{2}/(\rm several)]<\mu<(\rm several)\Lambda_{2}
,  in the strong coupling regime
a(\mu\sim\Lambda_{2})\sim 1

and in the background of lighter fields;  b) the
unsuppressed  non-perturbative instanton contributions from
the broken color
subgroup
SU(2N_{c}-N_{F})\rightarrow U^{2N_{c}-N_{F}-1}(1)
which are operating at the scale

\mu\sim\Lambda_{2}
. Without instantons, different pure perturbative loop
contributions to the superpotential cancel in the sum, but the
additional instanton contributions spoil this cancelation. See also
the footnote
54
. Compare with the absence of such terms in
sections 43.1, 43.2, see (
43.1.4
),(
43.2.3
), when

\langle X^{adj}_{SU(N_{c})}\rangle\sim m\gg\Lambda_{2}
is higgsed in the weak
coupling regime, resulting in
SU(N_{c})\rightarrow SU({\rm n}_{1})\times U(1)\times SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})
with
unbroken at this scale the non-Abelian
{\cal N}=2\,\,SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})
SYM part. The instanton contributions are then power
suppressed. They originate only from this
{\cal N}=2
SYM part and
operate in the lower energy strong coupling region at the scale

\mu\sim\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle\ll m
, after the color breaking
SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})\rightarrow U^{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}-1}(1)
by higgsed
\langle X^{adj}_{SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}\rangle\sim\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle
, see (
41.1.2
).

The way the parameter ”m” enters (
41.1.2
) can be understood as
follows. This parameter ”m” in the original superpotential
{\mathcal{W}}_{\rm matter}
in (
40.1
) can be considered as a soft scalar

U^{(0)}(1)
background field coupled universally to the
SU(N_{c})

singlet scalar electric baryon current,
\delta{\mathcal{W}}_{B}=mJ_{B}
, and this
current looks at the scale
\mu\gg\Lambda_{2}
as
J_{B}={\rm Tr}\,({\overline{Q}}Q)_{N_{c}}
. But at the lower energy scale
\mu\ll\Lambda_{2}
only lighter
particles with masses
\ll\Lambda_{2}
and with a nonzero electric baryon
charge contribute to
J_{B}
, these are
{\rm Tr}\,({\overline{Q}}Q)_{{\overline{N}}_{c}}

and
\sum_{j=1}^{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{\overline{D}}_{j}D_{j}
(see bellow). Moreover, the field

a_{1}
plays a similar role, but its couplings are different for two
different color sectors of
SU(N_{c})
, see
(
41.1.1
),(
41.1.2
).

The terms with
\delta_{i},\,\,i=1\,...\,4\,,\,\,\delta_{i}=O(1)
, arose
in (
41.1.2
) from integrating out heaviest original fields with
masses
\sim\Lambda_{2}
in the soft background of lighter
X^{\rm adj}_{SU({\overline{N}}_{c})},\,\,a_{1}
and
(m-c_{1}a_{1})
fields. These heavy fields
are : charged
SU(2N_{c}-N_{F})
adjoints with masses

(\Lambda_{j}-\Lambda_{i}),\,\,\Lambda_{j}\sim\omega^{\,j-1}\Lambda_{2}
, the
heavy quarks with
SU(2N_{c}-N_{F})
colors and masses
\Lambda_{j}-(m-c_{1}a_{1})
,
and heavy adjoint hybrids
SU(N_{c})/[SU({\overline{N}}_{c})\times SU(2N_{c}-N_{F})\times U^{(1)}(1)]
with masses
\Lambda_{j}-[\sqrt{2}X^{\rm adj}_{SU({\overline{N}}_{c})}+(1-c_{1})a_{1}]
. Note that terms like
\mu_{\rm x}\Lambda_{2}^{2},\,\,\mu_{\rm x}a_{1}\Lambda_{2}
and
\mu_{\rm x}(m-c_{1}a_{1})\Lambda_{2}
cannot appear in (
41.1.2
),
they are forbidden by the unbroken
Z_{2N_{c}-N_{F}\geq 2}
symmetry.

The above described picture is more or less typical, the only really
non-trivial point concerns the quantum numbers of
2N_{c}-N_{F}
dyons

D_{j}
. Therefore, we describe now some necessary requirements to them.

a)  First, the
SU({\overline{N}}_{c})
part of (
41.1.2
),
due to its own
internal dynamics
, ensures finally the right pattern of the
spontaneous flavor symmetry breaking
U(N_{F})\rightarrow U({\rm n}_{1})\times U({\rm n}_{2})

and the right multiplicity of vacua,
N_{\rm br2}=({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})C^{\,{\rm n}_{1}}_{N_{F}}
, see below. Therefore, the quantum numbers of these

2N_{c}-N_{F}
dyons
D_{j}
should not spoil the right dynamics in the
SU({\overline{N}}_{c})

sector.

b)  These quantum numbers should be such that the lower energy
Lagrangian respects the discrete
Z_{2N_{c}-N_{F}\geq 2}
symmetry. This fixes,
in particular, the number
2N_{c}-N_{F}
of these dyons because
Z_{2N_{c}-N_{F}\geq 2}
transformations interchange them among one another. Notice also
that the quantum numbers of these dyons
D_{i}
formed at the scale

\mu\sim\Lambda_{2}
cannot know about properties of further color or flavor
breaking in the
SU({\overline{N}}_{c})
color part at much lower scales
\mu\sim m\ll\Lambda_{2}
or
\mu\sim(\mu_{\rm x}m)^{1/2}\ll m
, i.e. they do not know the
number
{\rm n}_{1}
.

c)  According to M.Shifman and A.Yung, see e.g.
[
48
]
,
the massive   diagonal quarks
{\overline{Q}}^{i}_{i},\,Q^{i}_{i},\,i=1...2N_{c}-N_{F}
with

SU(2N_{c}-N_{F})
colors and masses
\sim\Lambda_{2}
combine at the scale

\mu\sim\Lambda_{2}
with
SU(2N_{c}-N_{F})
appropriate massive flavorless
magnetic (anti)monopoles and form
2N_{c}-N_{F}
massless dyons
{\overline{D}}_{i},D_{i}
in (
41.1.2
). But for quarks with equal own masses

"m"
the non-Abelian flavor symmetry
SU(N_{F})
is
not broken
by
higgsed
\langle X_{SU(2N_{c}-N_{F})}\rangle\sim\Lambda_{2}
at the scale
\mu\sim\Lambda_{2}
,
and
flavorless monopoles cannot distinguish between quarks with
different
SU(N_{F})
flavors. Therefore, such dyons will really have
the same flavor as quarks, i.e. they will form the (anti)fundamental
representation of global
SU(N_{F})
.

But these
2N_{c}-N_{F}
dyons
{\overline{D}}_{i},D_{i}
in (
41.1.2
) have to be

SU(N_{F})
flavor singlets. Otherwise, because they all are higgsed at
the scale
\mu\sim(\mu_{\rm x}\Lambda_{2})^{1/2}\neq 0
, this will result in a wrong
pattern of the spontaneous non-Abelian flavor symmetry breaking

U(N_{F})\rightarrow U(2N_{c}-N_{F})\times U(2N_{F}-2N_{c})
instead of the right
one
U(N_{F})\rightarrow U({\rm n}_{1})\times U({\rm n}_{2})
. Besides, there will be

N_{F}(2N_{c}-N_{F})
these dyons
D
, this is clearly too much.

In particular, the arguments in
[
48
]
are based on considering
the unequal mass quarks with
\delta m\neq 0
, the
“outside”  region
\delta m\gg\Lambda_{2}
and “inside”  region

\delta m\ll\Lambda_{2}
and the path between them going through
Argyres-Douglas point.

We would like to imphasize that these “outside”  and “inside”
regions are
completely separated
by so-called “wall crossing curves”.
And there is no such path in the complex plane
\delta m/\Lambda_{2}

which connects the “outside”  and “inside”  regions and does
not cross this “wall crossing curve”. And the path going through
Argyres-Douglas point is not the exclusion.

The quantum numbers of light particles jump generically when
the generic path crosses the “wall crossing curve” and have to be
carefully considered separately in the “outside”  and “inside”
regions. And this questions the statements of M. Shifman and
A.Yung about quantum numbers of light particles.

d)  Their magnetic quantum numbers should be such that they are
mutually local with respect to all original pure electric particles in
the
SU({\overline{N}}_{c})
sector. Otherwise, in particular, because all these
dyons and all original pure electric quarks from
SU({\overline{N}}_{c})
have
nonzero
U^{(1)}(1)
charges, and because all dyons are higgsed,

\langle{\overline{D}}_{j}\rangle=\langle D_{j}\rangle\sim(\mu_{\rm x}\Lambda_{2})^{1/2}
, if
the magnetic
U^{(1)}(1)
charge of these dyons were nonzero, this
would lead to confinement of all quarks from the
SU({\overline{N}}_{c})
sector with
the string   tension
\sigma^{1/2}\sim(\mu_{\rm x}\Lambda_{2})^{1/2}
. E.g., see below,
all
N_{F}
flavors of confined light electric quarks with
SU({\rm n}_{1})

colors would decouple then in any case at the scale

\mu<(\mu_{\rm x}\Lambda_{2})^{1/2}
, the flavor symmetry
U(N_{F})
would remain
unbroken in the whole
SU({\overline{N}}_{c})
sector, while the remained
{\cal N}=2\,\,SU({\rm n}_{1})
SYM would give the additional wrong factor
{\rm n}_{1}
in
the multiplicity.   All this is clearly unacceptable. This excludes the
variant with the nonzero
U^{(1)}(1)
magnetic charge (and also with
non-zero
SU({\overline{N}}_{c})
magnetic charges) of these dyons. Similarly, when
considering the
U(N_{c})=SU(N_{c})\times U^{(0)}(1)
theory in section
41.2 below, this also requires the
SU(N_{c})
baryon charge of these
dyons to be pure electric. For the same reasons, the magnetic charges
of these dyons cannot be
SU(N_{c})/[SU({\overline{N}}_{c})\times SU(2N_{c}-N_{F})
hybrids. On
the whole, to be mutually local with the whole
SU({\overline{N}}_{c})
part, the
magnetic parts of all charges of these dyons have to be
SU(2N_{c}-N_{F})

root-like (i.e.
SU(2N_{c}-N_{F})
adjoints most naturally).

e)  If the
U^{(1)}(1)
charge of these
2N_{c}-N_{F}
dyons
D_{j}
were zero
this would correspond then to the first term of
{\mathcal{W}}_{D}
in
(
41.1.2
),(
41.1.5
) of the form
m\sum_{j}^{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{\overline{D}}_{j}D_{j}
. They would not be coupled then with the
U^{(1)}(1)
multiplet
(
41.1.1
) and this would lead to the internal inconsistency, see below.

The case with zero
SU(N_{c})
baryon charge of dyons, i.e. with the
first term of
{\mathcal{W}}_{D}
of the form
[\,-c_{1}a_{1}\sum_{j=1}^{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{\overline{D}}_{j}D_{j}\,]
in (
41.1.2
), (
41.1.5
), will lead to wrong
values of quark condensates, see page 166 below

Finally, about the case when this first term of
{\mathcal{W}}_{D}
in
(
41.1.2
) equals zero. The whole dyon sector will be then
completely decoupled from the
SU({\overline{N}}_{c})\times U^{(1)}(1)
sector, so
that
\langle{\overline{D}}_{j}D_{j}\rangle
will not include corrections
containing
\langle\Lambda^{SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\,\rangle
.   And this will be wrong, because the values of
\langle{\overline{D}}_{j}D_{j}\rangle
obtained
independently
from the roots of the curve
(
40.2
) contain such corrections, see (
AX.15
).

Moreover, counting degrees
of freedom in the
SU({\overline{N}}_{c})\times U^{(1)}(1)
sector (see below), it
is   seen that one
{\cal N}=1
photon multiplet will remain exactly
massless in this
SU({\overline{N}}_{c})
sector at small
\mu_{\rm x}\neq 0

(while its
{\cal N}=2
scalar partner will have very small mass
\sim\mu_{\rm x}

only due to breaking
{\cal N}=2\rightarrow{\cal N}=1
at small
\mu_{\rm x}\neq 0
).

Besides, when even one out of
U^{2N_{c}-N_{F}-1}(1)
Abelian photon
multiplets   is not coupled with the whole set of
2N_{c}-N_{F}
dyons then,
due to unbroken
Z_{2N_{c}-N_{F}}
discrete symmetry, all
2N_{c}-N_{F}-1

Abelian multiplets will not be coupled. In any case (not even speaking
about other problems), at   least all
U^{2N_{c}-N_{F}-1}(1)
photons
multiplets will remain then massless even at
\mu_{\rm x}\neq 0
.

According to our assumption “B” (see Introduction), all variants with
extra (i.e. in addition to standard massless Nambu-Goldston particles
at
m_{i}=m
and
\mu_{\rm x}\neq 0
,  in
SU(N_{c})
theories
are excluded.

f)  The regime in the
SU(2N_{c}-N_{F})
color sector at low energies is IR
free. As follows from the curve (
40.2
), see (
AX.15
)),
all
2N_{c}-N_{F}
these BPS dyons are higgsed simultaneously in the ground
state at small
\mu_{\rm x}\neq 0
. And for this to be possible,
they all
have to be   massless at
\mu_{\rm x}\rightarrow 0
and to be mutually local
(so
that
\langle a_{D,j}\rangle=0
, see (
41.1.5
),(
41.1.6
)
below).

g)  No one of
U^{2N_{c}-N_{F}-1}(1)
charges of these light BPS dyons
D_{j}

can   be
pure electric
. Indeed, since all
SU(2N_{c}-N_{F})
adjoint
\langle a_{j}\rangle\sim\Lambda_{2}
in (
41.1.1
),   all BPS solitons with some pure
electric
U^{2N_{c}-N_{F}-1}(1)
charges will have large masses
\sim\Lambda_{2}
in
this case.

Therefore, as a result, these mutually local
2N_{c}-N_{F}
BPS dyons
D_{j},{\overline{D}}_{j}
, massless at
\mu_{\rm x}\rightarrow 0
, should have nonzero: i)
pure
electric
both
SU(N_{c})
baryon and
U^{(1)}(1)
charges; ii) on the
whole, all
2N_{c}-N_{F}-1
nonzero independent Abelian charges of the broken

SU(2N_{c}-N_{F})
subgroup, with their
SU(2N_{c}-N_{F})
adjoint magnetic parts. Then
they will be mutually local with the whole
SU({\overline{N}}_{c})
sector, and
coupled with
"m"
and with the electric
U^{(1)}(1)
multiplet, and
with all
U^{2N_{c}-N_{F}-1}(1)
Abelian multiplets,
see(
41.1.1
),(
41.1.2
). There will be no massless
particles at
\mu_{\rm x}\neq 0,\,m_{i}=m\neq 0,\,\,\delta_{ij}\neq 0

and the multiplicity and the pattern of flavor
symmetry breaking will be right, see below.

2)  The second stage is the breaking
SU({\overline{N}}_{c})\rightarrow SU(n_{1})\times U^{(2)}(1)\times SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-n_{1})
at the lower scale
\mu\sim m\ll\Lambda_{2}

in the weak coupling regime
, see (
41.1.5
),(
41.1.6
)
below,
1\leq{\rm n}_{1}<{\overline{N}}_{c}=N_{F}-N_{c}
, (this stage is qualitatively similar
to those in section 43.1)

\displaystyle\sqrt{2}\,X^{(2)}_{U(1)}=a_{2}\,{\rm diag}(\,\underbrace{\,1}_{{\rm n}_{1}}\,;\,\underbrace{\,c_{2}}_{{\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}\,;\,\underbrace{0}_{2N_{c}-N_{F}}\,),\hskip 11.74988ptc_{2}=-\,\frac{{\rm n}_{1}}{{\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\langle a_{2}\rangle=\frac{N_{c}}{{\overline{N}}_{c}}\,m\,.

(41.1.3)

As a result, all original electric quarks with
SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})
colors
and
N_{F}
flavors have masses
m_{2}=\langle m-a_{1}-c_{2}a_{2}\rangle=m\,N_{c}/({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})
, and all original electric

SU({\overline{N}}_{c})
adjoint hybrids
SU({\overline{N}}_{c})/[SU({\rm n}_{1})\times SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})\times U(1)]
also have masses
(1-c_{2})\langle a_{2}\rangle=m_{2}
, and so all
these particles decouple as heavy at scales
\mu\lesssim m
. But the
original electric quarks with
SU({\rm n}_{1})
colors and
N_{F}
flavors have
masses
\langle m-a_{1}-a_{2}\rangle=0
, they remain massless at
\mu_{\rm x}\rightarrow 0

and survive at
\mu\lesssim m
. Therefore, there remain two lighter
non-Abelian sectors
SU({\rm n}_{1})\times SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})
. As for the first one,
it is the IR free
{\cal N}=2\,\,SU({\rm n}_{1})
SQCD with
N_{F}
flavors of
original electric quarks massless at
\mu_{\rm x}\rightarrow 0\,,\,\,N_{F}>2{\rm n}_{1},\,\,1\leq{\rm n}_{1}<{\overline{N}}_{c}\,
. As for the second one, it is the
{\cal N}=2\,\,SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})
SYM with the scale factor
\Lambda^{SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}
of its gauge coupling, compare with

\langle S\rangle_{N_{c}}
in (
41.1
),

\displaystyle\Bigl(\Lambda^{SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\Bigr)^{2({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}=\frac{\Lambda_{SU({\overline{N}}_{c})}^{2{\overline{N}}_{c}-N_{F}}(m-a_{1}-c_{2}a_{2})^{N_{F}}}{[\,(1-c_{2})a_{2}\,]^{\,2{\rm n}_{1}}}\,,

(41.1.4)

\langle\Lambda^{SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle^{2}=m^{2}_{2}\Bigl(\frac{m_{2}}{\Lambda_{SU({\overline{N}}_{c})}}\Bigr)^{\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{{\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}}\ll m^{2}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\langle S\rangle_{{\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}=\mu_{\rm x}(1+\delta_{2})\langle\Lambda^{SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=2\,SYM}\rangle^{2}\,,

m_{2}=\langle m-a_{1}-c_{2}a_{2}\rangle=(1-c_{2})\langle a_{2}\rangle=\frac{N_{c}}{{\rm n}_{2}-N_{c}}\,m=-\hat{m}\,,

where
\Lambda_{SU({\overline{N}}_{c})}
is the scale factor of the
SU({\overline{N}}_{c})
gauge
coupling at the scale

in (

),
after integrating out heaviest particles with masses
\sim\Lambda_{2}
(it

3)  At the third stage, to avoid unphysical

g^{2}(\mu<\langle\Lambda^{SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\,\rangle)<0
of UV free

in a well
known way
[

]
,

. All original

in the overall multiplicity of considered br2

SYM. Besides, the appearance of two single roots
with
(e^{+}-e^{-})\sim\langle\Lambda^{SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle
, of the curve (
40.2
) in these br2 vacua is
connected just with this
{\cal N}=2\,\,SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})
SYM
[
31
]
.
Other

4)  All
2N_{c}-N_{F}
dyons
{\overline{D}}_{j},D_{j}
are higgsed at the scale

\sim(\mu_{\rm x}\Lambda_{2})^{1/2}
in the weak coupling regime:
\,\,\langle D_{j}\rangle=\langle{\overline{D}}_{j}\rangle\sim(\mu_{\rm x}\Lambda_{2})^{1/2}\gg(\mu_{\rm x}m)^{1/2}

2N_{c}-N_{F}
long

U^{(1)}(1)
with its scalar

a_{1}
), all with masses
[\,g_{D}(\mu\sim(\mu_{\rm x}\Lambda_{2})^{1/2})\ll 1\,](\mu_{\rm x}\Lambda_{2})^{1/2}
(for simplicity, we ignore below logarithmically
small factors
\sim g
in similar cases, these are implied where
needed). No massless particles remain in this sector at
\mu_{\rm x}\neq 0
.
And there remain no lighter particles in this sector at lower energies.
The
(2N_{c}-N_{F})
-set of these higgsed dyons with the

adjoint

SU(2N_{c}-N_{F})
pure electrically charged particles with largest

\sim\Lambda_{2}
.   Therefore, all these heaviest electric particles are
weakly  , confined, the string
tension is
\sigma^{1/2}_{D}\sim(\mu_{\rm x}\Lambda_{2})^{1/2}
.
This confinement is weak in the sense that the tension of the
confining string is much smaller than particle masses,

\sigma^{1/2}_{D}\sim(\mu_{\rm x}\Lambda_{2})^{1/2}\ll\Lambda_{2}\,
. All these heaviest
confined particles form a large number of hadrons with masses

\sim\Lambda_{2}
.

5)
{\rm n}_{1}
out of
N_{F}
electric quarks of IR free

\mu\sim(\mu_{\rm x}\,m)^{1/2}
in the weak coupling region, with
\langle{\overline{Q}}_{k}^{\,a}\rangle=\langle Q^{k}_{a}\rangle\sim\delta^{k}_{a}\,(m\,\mu_{\rm x})^{1/2},\,\,a=1,...,{\rm n}_{1},\,\,k=1,...,N_{F}

SU({\rm n}_{1})\times U^{(2)}(1)
is broken and
{\rm n}_{1}^{2}
s;ightly broken
long

multiplets of
massive electric gluons are formed (including
U^{(2)}(1)
with its
scalar
a_{2}
), all with masses
\sim(m\,\mu_{\rm x})^{1/2}\ll(\mu_{\rm x}\Lambda_{2})^{1/2}
.
The global   flavor symmetry is broken spontaneously
only at this
stage
,

, and
2{\rm n}_{1}{\rm n}_{2}
(complex)
massless Nambu-Goldstone multiplets remain in this sector at lower
energies (in essence, these are quarks
Q^{k}_{a},\,{\overline{Q}}^{\,a}_{k},\,a=1,...,{\rm n}_{1},\,\,k={\rm n}_{1}+1,...,N_{F}
). This is a reason
for the origin of the factor
C^{\,{\rm n}_{1}}_{N_{F}}
in multiplicity of
these br2 vacua. (See also the text somewhat below (
41.1.6
)
about
(\delta m)_{ij}
).

6)
{\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}-1
magnetic monopoles are higgsed at the lowest
scale,
\langle{\overline{M}}_{\rm n}\rangle=\langle M_{\rm n}\rangle\sim(\mu_{\rm x}\langle\Lambda^{SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle)_{,}^{1/2}
and
{\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}-1
long
{\cal N}=2

multiplets of massive

photons are formed, all with
masses
\sim(\mu_{\rm x}\langle\Lambda^{SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle)^{1/2}\ll(\mu_{\rm x}m)^{1/2}
. All original pure electrically
charged particles with non-singlet
SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})
charges and
masses either
\sim m
or
\sim\langle\Lambda^{SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle
are weakly confined, the string

\sigma^{1/2}_{\rm SYM}\sim\,(\mu_{\rm x}\langle\Lambda^{SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle)^{1/2}
. No
massless particles remain in this sector at
\mu_{\rm x}\neq 0,\,m\neq 0
.

As a result of all described above, the lowest energy superpotential
at the scale
\mu=\langle\Lambda^{SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\,\rangle/\\
(\rm several)
, and e.g. at smallest
0<\mu_{\rm x}\ll\langle\Lambda^{SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle^{2}/\Lambda_{2}
, can be written in
these br2 vacua as

\displaystyle{\mathcal{W}}^{\,\rm low}_{\rm tot}={\mathcal{W}}^{\,(SYM)}_{SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}+{\mathcal{W}}^{\,\rm low}_{\rm matter}+\dots\,,\hskip 11.74988pt{\mathcal{W}}^{\,\rm low}_{\rm matter}={\mathcal{W}}_{SU({\rm n}_{1})}+{\mathcal{W}}_{D}+{\mathcal{W}}_{a_{1},\,a_{2}}\,,

(41.1.5)

{\mathcal{W}}^{\,(SYM)}_{SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}=({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})\widetilde{\mu}_{\rm x}\Bigl(\Lambda^{SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\Bigr)^{2}+{\mathcal{W}}^{\,(M)}_{SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\widetilde{\mu}_{\rm x}=\mu_{\rm x}(1+\delta_{2})\,,

{\mathcal{W}}^{\,(M)}_{SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}=-\sum_{n=1}^{{\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}-1}a_{M,\rm n}\Biggl[\,{\overline{M}}_{\rm n}M_{\rm n}+\widetilde{\mu}_{\rm x}\Lambda^{SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\Biggl(1+O\Bigl(\frac{\langle\Lambda^{SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle}{m}\Bigr)\Biggr)d_{\rm n}\,\Biggr]\,,

{\mathcal{W}}_{SU({\rm n}_{1})}=(m-a_{1}-a_{2})\,{\rm Tr}\,({\overline{Q}}Q)_{{\rm n}_{1}}-\,{\rm Tr}\,({\overline{Q}}\sqrt{2}X^{\rm adj}_{SU({\rm n}_{1})}Q)_{{\rm n}_{1}}+\widetilde{\mu}_{\rm x}{\rm Tr}\,(X^{\rm adj}_{SU({\rm n}_{1})})^{2}\,,

{\mathcal{W}}_{D}=(m-c_{1}a_{1})\sum_{j=1}^{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{\overline{D}}_{j}D_{j}-\sum_{j=1}^{2N_{c}-N_{F}}a_{D,j}\,{\overline{D}}_{j}D_{j}\,-\,\mu_{\rm x}\Lambda_{2}\sum_{j=1}^{2N_{c}-N_{F}}\omega^{j-1}\,a_{D,j}+\mu_{\rm x}L\sum_{j=1}^{2N_{c}-N_{F}}a_{D,j}\,,

{\mathcal{W}}_{a_{1},\,a_{2}}=\frac{\mu_{\rm x}}{2}(1+\delta_{1})\frac{{\overline{N}}_{c}N_{c}}{2N_{c}-N_{F}}a_{1}^{2}+\frac{\widetilde{\mu}_{\rm x}}{2}\frac{{\rm n}_{1}{\overline{N}}_{c}}{{\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}a_{2}^{2}+\mu_{\rm x}N_{c}\delta_{3}a_{1}(m-c_{1}a_{1})+\mu_{\rm x}N_{c}\delta_{4}(m-c_{1}a_{1})^{2}+O\Bigl(\mu_{\rm x}a_{D,j}^{2}\Bigr),

where coefficients
d_{i}=O(1)
in (
41.1.5
) are known from

[
31
]
, and dots in (
41.1.5
) denote smaller power
suppressed corrections (these are always implied and dots are omitted
below in the text), . It is useful to notice that the unbroken

Z_{2N_{c}-N_{F}\geq 2}
symmetry restricts strongly their possible
values. The matter is that e.g. power suppressed corrections in

{\mathcal{W}}_{a_{1}}
like
\sim\mu_{\rm x}a_{1}^{2}(a_{1}/\Lambda_{2})^{N}
originate from

SU(N_{c})\rightarrow SU({\overline{N}}_{c})\times U^{(1)}(1)\times SU(2N_{c}-N_{F})
at the scale

\sim\Lambda_{2}
and they do not know the number
{\rm n}_{1}
originating only at
much lower scales
\mu\sim m\ll\Lambda_{2}
. Then the unbroken

Z_{2N_{c}-N_{F}\geq 2}
symmetry requires that
N=(2N_{c}-N_{F})k,\,k=1,2,3...
.

Remind that charges of fields and parameters entering (
41.1.5
)
under
Z_{2N_{c}-N_{F}}=\exp\{i\pi/(2N_{c}-N_{F})\}
transformation are :

q_{\lambda}=q_{\theta}=1,\,\,q_{X_{SU({\rm n}_{1})}^{\rm adj}}=q_{a_{1}}=q_{a_{2}}=q_{a_{D,j}}=q_{a_{M,\rm n}}=q_{\rm m}=q_{L}=2,\,\,q_{Q}=q_{\overline{Q}}=q_{D_{j}}=q_{{\overline{D}}_{j}}=q_{M_{\rm n}}=q_{{\overline{M}}_{\rm n}}=q_{\Lambda_{2}}=0,\,\,q_{\mu_{\rm x}}=-2
. The non-trivial

Z_{2N_{c}-N_{F}\geq 2}
transformations change only numerations of dual
scalars
a_{D,j}
and dyons
D_{j},{\overline{D}}_{j}
in (
41.1.5
), so
that
\int d^{2}\theta\,{\mathcal{W}}_{\rm tot}^{\,\rm low}
is

Z_{2N_{c}-N_{F}}
-invariant.

All (massless at
\mu_{\rm x}\rightarrow 0
)
{\rm b}_{2}=2N_{c}-N_{F}
dyons
D_{j}
,
{\rm n}_{1}<{\overline{N}}_{c}

quarks
Q^{k}
and
{\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}-1
monopoles
M_{\rm n}
in (
41.1.5
)
are higgsed at
\mu_{\rm x}\neq 0
. As a result, we obtain from
(
41.1.5
) (neglecting power corrections)

\displaystyle\langle a_{1}\rangle=\frac{1}{c_{1}}\,m=-\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{{\overline{N}}_{c}}\,m\,,\,\,\langle a_{2}\rangle=\langle m-a_{1}\rangle=\frac{N_{c}}{{\overline{N}}_{c}}\,m\,,\,\,\langle X^{\rm adj}_{SU({\rm n}_{1})}\rangle=\langle a_{D,\,j}\rangle=\langle a_{M,\rm n}\rangle=0\,,

(41.1.6)

\langle{\overline{M}}_{\rm n}M_{\rm n}\rangle=\langle{\overline{M}}_{\rm n}\rangle\langle M_{\rm n}\rangle\approx-\widetilde{\mu}_{\rm x}\langle\Lambda^{SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle d_{\rm n}\,,\,\,d_{\rm n}=O(1),\hskip 11.74988pt\langle{\overline{D}}_{j}\,D_{j}\rangle=\langle{\overline{D}}_{j}\rangle\langle D_{j}\rangle\approx-\mu_{\rm x}\Lambda_{2}\,\omega^{j-1}\,,

\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{{\rm n}_{1}}=\langle{\overline{Q}}^{1}_{1}\rangle\langle Q^{1}_{1}\rangle\approx\widetilde{\mu}_{\rm x}\frac{{\overline{N}}_{c}}{{\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}\langle a_{2}\rangle\approx\widetilde{\mu}_{\rm x}\frac{N_{c}}{{\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}\,m,\,\,\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{{\rm n}_{1}}=\sum_{a=1}^{{\rm n}_{1}}\langle{\overline{Q}}^{\,a}_{2}\rangle\langle Q^{2}_{a}\rangle=0,\,\,\langle S\rangle_{{\rm n}_{1}}=0

(the dyon condensates are dominated by terms
\sim\mu_{\rm x}\Lambda_{2}
plus smaller
terms
\sim\mu_{\rm x}m
, see Appendix B).

We have to explain at this point the meaning of notations used e.g.
in  (
41.1.5
),(
41.1.6
) for the superpotential and mean
values   written for the theory with the upper cutoff
(\mu_{\rm x}m)^{1/2}\ll\mu_{uv}=\langle\Lambda^{SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle/(\rm several)\ll m
. By definition, for any operator

O,\,\,\langle O\rangle
denotes its
total mean vacuum value

integrated from sufficiently high energies (formally, from the very
high UV cutoff
M_{UV}
for the elementary fields and from
\mu_{\rm sol}
for composite solitons formed at the scale
\sim\mu_{\rm sol}
)
down to
\mu^{\rm lowest}_{\rm cut}=0
, i.e.
\langle O\rangle\equiv\langle O\rangle^{M_{UV}\,{\rm or}\,\mu_{\rm sol}}_{\mu^{\rm lowest}_{\rm cut}=0}
. Therefore, we have, strictly
speaking, to write e.g. for the lower energy superpotential in
(
41.1.5
):
\,a_{1,2}=\langle a_{1,2}\rangle^{M_{UV}}_{\mu^{\rm low}_{\rm cut}=\mu_{uv}}+(\hat{a}_{1,2})^{\mu_{uv}}
, with
\mu^{\rm low}_{\rm cut}=\mu_{uv}=\langle\Lambda^{SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle/(\rm several)
, and the remaining soft parts
(\hat{a}_{1,2})^{\mu_{uv}}
of operators
a_{1,2}
with the upper energy
cutoff
\mu_{uv}\ll m
. But in the case considered, with higgsed

\langle a_{1,2}\rangle\sim m\ll\Lambda_{2}
, these their total mean values

originate and saturate
in the range of scales
m/(\rm several)<\mu<(\rm several)m
, because otherwise all
SU({\overline{N}}_{c})
quarks
and all dyons would have masses
\sim m
. They all will decouple then
as heavy at the scale
\mu<m/(\rm several)
and this will be clearly
wrong for these br2-vacua (the multiplicity will be wrong, the flavor
symmetry will remain unbroken, the number of charged BPS particles
massless at
\mu_{\rm x}\rightarrow 0
will be wrong, etc.). Therefore,
\langle a_{1,2}\rangle^{M_{UV}}_{\mu_{uv}}=\langle a_{1,2}\rangle^{M_{UV}}_{\mu^{\rm lowest}_{\rm cut}=0}\equiv\langle a_{1,2}\rangle=\langle a_{1,2}\rangle^{(\rm several)m}_{m/(\rm several)}
, and
\langle{\hat{a}}\rangle^{\mu_{uv}}_{\mu^{\rm lowest}_{\rm cut}=0}=0
. For this reason, only in order to simplify
all notations (here and in most cases below in the text) all is
written as in (
41.1.5
),(
41.1.6
). We hope that the
meaning of operators and mean values entering all formulas will
be clear in each case from the accompanying text.

Now, in a few words, it is not difficult to check that the variant of
(
41.1.5
) with the first term of
{\mathcal{W}}_{D}
of the form

m\sum_{j=1}^{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{\overline{D}}_{j}D_{j}
(i.e. with zero
U^{(1)}(1)
charge
of dyons) results in
\langle\partial{\mathcal{W}}^{\,\rm low}_{\rm matter}/\partial L\rangle=\mu_{\rm x}m(2N_{c}-N_{F})/2\neq 0
, and this is the
internal inconsistency. Another way, with such a form of the first
term of
{\mathcal{W}}_{D}
, one obtains from
\langle{\partial{\mathcal{W}}_{D}}/{\partial{\overline{D}}_{j}}\rangle=0
:
\langle a_{D,j}\rangle=m,\,\,\sum_{j}\langle a_{D,j}\rangle=(2N_{c}-N_{F})m\neq 0
, and this is wrong.

The
2N_{c}-N_{F}
unequal double roots
e^{(D)}_{j}\approx\omega^{j-1}\Lambda_{2}
of
the curve (
40.2
) correspond to BPS dyons formed at the scale
\mu\sim\Lambda_{2}
. For slightly unequal mass quarks with
(\delta m)_{ij}\neq 0

(see just below), together with
{\rm n}_{1}
unequal double roots
e^{(Q)}_{k}=-m_{k}
of original pure electric quarks from

{\cal N}=2\,\,SU({\rm n}_{1})
SQCD, and
{\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}-1
unequal double roots
of
SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})
of adjoint pure magnetic monopoles from
{\cal N}=2\,\,SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})
SYM (formed at the scale

\mu\sim\langle\Lambda^{SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle
),
they constitute the total set of
N_{c}-1
double roots of the curve
(
40.2
) at
\mu_{\rm x}\rightarrow 0
.. There are no
additional
massless at

\mu_{\rm x}\rightarrow 0
charged BPS solitons because there will be then too
many double roots of the curve (
40.2
).

The short additional explanations will be useful at this point. As was
pointed out in Introduction, the spectral curve (
40.2
) can be
used, in general, to determine multiplicities and charges of massless
charged BPS particles in the formal limit
\mu_{\rm x}\rightarrow 0
only. But in cases
with equal mass quarks as in br2 vacua considered
in this section (and in many other vacua), some additional infrared
regularization is needed to really have only
N_{c}-1
exactly massless
at
\mu_{\rm x}\rightarrow 0
charged BPS particles and corresponding them
N_{c}-1

well defined
unequal double roots
of the
SU(N_{c})
curve
(
40.2
). We have in mind that all charged particles of the

{\cal N}=2\,\,SU({\rm n}_{1})
subgroup will be massless at
\mu_{\rm x}\rightarrow 0
for
equal mass quarks, this corresponds to
{\rm n}_{1}

equal double roots

e^{(Q)}_{k}=-m,\,\,k=1...{\rm n}_{1}
of the curve (
40.2
). To have
really only
{\rm n}_{1}
exactly massless at
\mu_{\rm x}\rightarrow 0
charged BPS particles
in this
SU({\rm n}_{1})
sector, we have to split slightly the quark masses.
For instance, in br2 vacua of this section it is sufficient to split
slightly the masses of quarks from color
SU({\rm n}_{1})
with the first
{\rm n}_{1}

flavors. I.e. :
m_{k}=m+{\delta m}_{k},\,\,k=1...N_{F},\,\,{\delta m}_{k}\sim{\delta m}_{n},\,\,\sum_{k=1}^{{\rm n}_{1}}{\delta m}_{k}=0
, for
k,n=1...{\rm n}_{1}
, while
{\delta m}_{k}=0
for
k={\rm n}_{1}+1\,...\,N_{F}
. The mass splittings
{\delta m}_{k},\,\,k=1...{\rm n}_{1}
are arbitrary small but fixed to be unequal and
nonzero,
(\delta m)_{ij}=(\delta m_{i}-\delta m_{j})\neq 0
at

.
In this case,
\langle X^{\rm adj}_{SU({\rm n}_{1})}\rangle

in   (
41.1.6
) will be nonzero,
\langle{\sqrt{2}}X^{\rm adj}_{SU({\rm n}_{1})}\rangle={\rm diag}(\,{\delta m}_{1}\,...\,{\delta m}_{{\rm n}_{1}}\,)
, and
SU(N_{F})\rightarrow U^{N_{F}-1}(1)
due to not spontaneous
but explicit breaking of the global

flavor symmetry by

(\delta m)_{ij}\neq 0
. The massless charged
BPS particles at

will be only
{\rm n}_{1}
quarks

(higgsed at

),
while all other charged BPS particles of color

will acquire nonzero masses

.
And the

) will have just
{\rm n}_{1}
slightly
split quark double roots

. And this
shows now most clearly that
there are no other charged BPS
solitons massless at
\mu_{\rm x}\rightarrow 0
. This infrared regularization will be
always implied when we will speak about only

) at

(or about

(And the same for the

),(

As it should be, for equal mass quarks, there remain

The calculation of the leading power correction

\sim\mu_{\rm x}\langle\Lambda^{SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle^{2}/m
to the value of

independent
