only for BPS particles. This agreement confirms
in a non-trivial way a self-consistency of the whole approach and BPS
properties of dyons and original quarks
Q^{i},{\overline{Q}}_{j}
.

According to reasonings given above in this section, the quarks with
all
N_{F}
flavors,
SU(2N_{c}-N_{F})
colors, and masses
\sim\Lambda_{2}
are
too   heavy and too short ranged and cannot form a coherent condensate
for this reason. In other words, they are
not higgsed
, i.e.

\langle Q^{i}_{a}\rangle=\langle{\overline{Q}}^{\,a}_{j}\rangle=0,\,\,a={\overline{N}}_{c}+1...N_{c}
. Indeed, in the range from high energies down to

\mu^{\rm low}_{\rm cut}=(\mu_{\rm x}\Lambda_{2})^{1/2}
,
for independently
moving
heavy quarks
{\overline{Q}}^{\,a}_{j}
and
Q_{a}^{i}
with
SU(2N_{c}-N_{F})

colors, all their physical (i.e. path-dependent) phases induced by
interactions with the
light

\,U^{(1)}(1)\times U^{2N_{c}-N_{F}-1}(1)

photons still fluctuate freely, so that the mean values of quark
fields integrated over this energy range, as well as the mean values
of factorizable parts of local quarks bilinears, look as (the
non-factorizable parts are also zero in this energy range, see below)

\displaystyle\langle{\overline{Q}}^{\,a}_{j}\rangle_{\mu^{\rm low}_{\rm cut}}=\langle Q_{a}^{i}\rangle_{\mu^{\rm low}_{\rm cut}}=\langle{\overline{Q}}^{\,a}_{j}Q_{a}^{i}\rangle^{\rm factor}_{\mu^{\rm low}_{\rm cut}}=0\,,\hskip 11.74988pta={\overline{N}}_{c}+1...N_{c}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\mu^{\rm low}_{\rm cut}=(\mu_{\rm x}\Lambda_{2})^{1/2}\,.\,\,

(41.1.16)

For this reason, at the scale
\mu_{\rm cut}=\Lambda_{2}/(\rm several)
where
all particles with masses
\sim\Lambda_{2}

decoupled already as heavy,
there are no any contributions to particle masses in the Lagrangian
(
41.1.2
) from mean values of heavy quark fields
,
\langle{\overline{Q}}^{\,a}_{j}\rangle_{\mu_{\rm cut}}=\langle Q_{a}^{i}\rangle_{\mu_{\rm cut}}=0,\,\,\mu_{\rm cut}=\Lambda_{2}/(\rm several)
. And after all heavy
particles decoupled at
\mu<\mu_{\rm cut}
, resulting in the
Lagrangian (
41.1.2
),
they themselves do not affect then
the behavior of lower energy theory
. (Really, because
{\rm n}_{2}>N_{c},\,{\rm n}_{1}<{\overline{N}}_{c}
in these br2 vacua, it is clear beforehand that the quarks

Q^{2},{\overline{Q}}_{2}
in
{\rm n}_{2}>N_{c}
equal condensates

\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{N_{c}}
are definitely not higgsed at all due to the
rank restrictions, because otherwise this will result in a wrong
pattern of flavor symmetry breaking. Higgsed are the light quarks

Q^{i}_{a},{\overline{Q}}^{\,a}_{i},\,\,i,a=1...{\rm n}_{1}
only, see (
41.1.14
)
and the end of section 41.4).

It is worth emphasizing also the following. The equality

\langle\,\partial{\mathcal{W}}/\partial\phi\rangle=0

is valid only when
all nonperturbative contributions are accounted for in the
superpotential

{\mathcal{W}}
. If nonperturbative contributions are smaller
than the perturbative ones, they can be neglected and then the
equality
\langle\,\partial{\mathcal{W}}_{\rm pert}/\partial\phi\rangle\approx 0
will be approximately right. If nonperturbative contributions are
of the same order as perturbative ones, then ignoring them will give
at best only the order of magnitude estimate. Remind that, by
definition for any operator
O=A-B
, the equality
\langle O\rangle=0\,
denotes the
total mean value
integrated from the appropriate
high energy down to
\mu^{\rm lowest}_{\rm cut}=0
. And following
from   it equality of
total mean values
of two operators,
\langle A\rangle=\langle B\rangle
,
does not mean in general that the
numerical values of
\langle A\rangle
and
\langle B\rangle

originate from the same energy regions
. The equality of
such
total mean values

\langle A\rangle=\langle B\rangle
means only
that, when integrated from the appropriate high energies down to zero,

their   numerical values
will be equal. In general,
the
numerical values of
\langle A\rangle
and
\langle B\rangle
can
originate from different energy regions
. This is the case with e.g.
(
41.1.23
), see below. And similarly in many cases in the text
below.

As it is seen from (
41.1
),(
41.4.1
), the
SU(2N_{c}-N_{F})

singlet parts of heavy quark
total mean values
,
\langle{\overline{Q}}_{j}Q^{i}\rangle_{2N_{c}-N_{F}}=\sum_{a={\overline{N}}_{c}+1}^{N_{c}}\langle{\overline{Q}}^{\,a}_{j}Q^{i}_{a}\rangle\sim\,\delta^{i}_{j}\,\mu_{\rm x}m\ll\mu_{\rm x}\Lambda_{2}
are of the same size
as condensates of light higgsed quarks

\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{{\overline{N}}_{c}}\approx\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{{\rm n}_{1}}=\langle{\overline{Q}}^{1}_{1}\rangle\langle Q^{1}_{1}\rangle\sim\mu_{\rm x}m
(the total mean values

\langle{\overline{Q}}_{i}Q^{i}\rangle_{{\overline{N}}_{c}}
of all flavors
i=1...N_{F}
of
quarks are holomorphic in
\mu_{\rm x}
and their values in
(
41.4.1
),(
41.4.5
),(
41.4.6
) are valid at smaller

\mu_{\rm x}\ll m
as well),

\displaystyle\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{2N_{c}-N_{F}}=\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{N_{c}}-\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{{\overline{N}}_{c}}\approx\mu_{\rm x}\,\hat{m}\Biggl[1+\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{{\rm n}_{2}-N_{c}}\Bigl(\frac{\hat{m}}{\Lambda_{2}}\Bigr)^{\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{{\rm n}_{2}-N_{c}}}\Biggr)\Biggr]\,,

(41.1.17)

\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{2N_{c}-N_{F}}=\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{N_{c}}-\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{{\overline{N}}_{c}}\approx\,-\mu_{\rm x}\,\hat{m}\Biggl[1+\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{{\rm n}_{2}-N_{c}}\Bigl(\frac{\hat{m}}{\Lambda_{2}}\Bigr)^{\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{{\rm n}_{2}-N_{c}}}\Biggr)\Biggr]\,,

\langle{\rm Tr\,}({\overline{Q}}Q)\rangle_{2N_{c}-N_{F}}={\rm n}_{2}\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{2N_{c}-N_{F}}+{\rm n}_{1}\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{2N_{c}-N_{F}}\approx({\rm n}_{2}-{\rm n}_{1})\mu_{\rm x}\,\hat{m}\Biggl[1+\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{{\rm n}_{2}-N_{c}}\Bigl(\frac{\hat{m}}{\Lambda_{2}}\Bigr)^{\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{{\rm n}_{2}-N_{c}}}\Biggr)\Biggr].

Besides, the total mean value of the flavor singlet part
\langle{\rm Tr\,}({\overline{Q}}Q)\rangle_{2N_{c}-N_{F}}
in (
41.1.17
) can be connected with the

\langle\Sigma_{D}\rangle
part of the dyon condensate,
\Sigma_{D}=\sum_{j=1}^{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{\overline{D}}_{j}D_{j}
. Considering ’m’ as the background
field, from (
40.1
),(
41.1.2
) and using
\delta_{3}=0
from
(
AX.4
),

\displaystyle\langle\,\frac{\partial}{\partial m}{\mathcal{W}}_{\rm matter}\rangle=\langle\,\frac{\partial}{\partial m}{\widehat{\mathcal{W}}}_{\rm matter}\rangle\rightarrow\langle{\rm Tr\,}({\overline{Q}}Q)\rangle_{2N_{c}-N_{F}}=\langle\Sigma_{D}\rangle\equiv\sum_{j=1}^{2N_{c}-N_{F}}\langle{\overline{D}}_{j}D_{j}\rangle=\sum_{j=1}^{2N_{c}-N_{F}}\langle{\overline{D}}_{j}\rangle\langle D_{j}\rangle.\hskip 11.74988pt\,\,\,\,

(41.1.18)

As for the value of
\langle\Sigma_{D}\rangle
in br2 vacua of the

SU(N_{c})
theory, it is obtained
independently
in Appendix B
using (
41.2.10
) and the values of roots of the curve
(
40.2
), see (
AX.7
),(
AX.15
)

\displaystyle\langle\Sigma_{D}\rangle=\sum_{j=1}^{2N_{c}-N_{F}}\langle{\overline{D}}_{j}\rangle\langle D_{j}\rangle\approx({\rm n}_{2}-{\rm n}_{1})\mu_{\rm x}\,\hat{m}\Biggl[1+\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{{\rm n}_{2}-N_{c}}\Bigl(\frac{\hat{m}}{\Lambda_{2}}\Bigr)^{\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{{\rm n}_{2}-N_{c}}}\Biggr)\Biggr]\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\hat{m}=\frac{N_{c}}{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{2}}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt

(41.1.19)

this agrees with (
41.1.17
),(
41.1.18
).

As an example, the
operator

{\rm Tr\,}(({\overline{Q}}Q))_{2N_{c}-N_{F}}
of heavy
quarks with masses
\sim\Lambda_{2}
in (
41.1.17
), when integrated over
the interval
(\rm several)m<\mu<(\rm several)\Lambda_{2}
, transforms in
general into a linear combination of three
operators
of light
fields, see (
41.1.1
)

\displaystyle\hskip-5.69054pt[\,{\rm Tr\,}(({\overline{Q}}Q))_{2N_{c}-N_{F}}\,]^{(\rm several)\Lambda_{2}}_{(\rm several)m}=\Biggl[f_{1}N_{c}\mu_{\rm x}(m-c_{1}a_{1})+f_{2}\Sigma_{D}+f_{3}{\rm Tr\,}(({\overline{Q}}Q))_{{\overline{N}}_{c}}\Bigr]_{,}^{(\rm several)m}\,\,\,\,f_{1,2,3}=O(1).\hskip 11.74988pt

(41.1.20)

This
operator expansion
relates then
the numerical total
mean values
of the left and right hand sides of (
41.1.20
). But
the total mean values of three operators in the right hand side of
(
41.1.20
) originate and saturate at parametrically different
energies.
\langle a_{1}\rangle
originates and saturates at
\mu\sim m
, as otherwise all dyons would have masses
\sim m\gg(\mu_{\rm x}\Lambda_{2})^{1/2}
. They will not be higgsed then but will decouple as
heavy at
\mu<m
.
2N_{c}-N_{F}
unequal double roots of the curve
(
40.2
) will be absent, while e.g. all
U^{(1)}(1)\times U^{2N_{c}-N_{F}}(1)
photons will remain massless, etc.

\langle\Sigma_{D}\rangle
originates and saturates at
\mu\sim(\mu_{\rm x}\Lambda_{2})^{1/2}\ll\langle\Lambda^{SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle\ll m
. And
\langle{\rm Tr\,}(({\overline{Q}}Q))_{{\overline{N}}_{c}}\rangle

originates and saturates from the sum of three regions,
\mu\sim m\ll\Lambda_{2}\,,\,\,\mu\sim\langle\Lambda^{SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle\ll m
, and
\mu\sim(\mu_{\rm x}m)^{1/2}\ll\langle\Lambda^{SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle
, see below
in this section. Besides, the
numerical total mean value
of the
right hand side in (
41.1.20
) equals
\langle\Sigma\rangle
,
see(
41.1.18
). And because
\langle m-c_{1}a_{1}\rangle=0
from
(
41.1.1
), we obtain:
f_{2}=1,\,f_{3}=0
.

The contributions to the heavy quark total mean values
\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1,2}\rangle_{2N_{c}-N_{F}}
also originate on the whole: a) from the
quantum loop effects at the scale
\mu\sim\Lambda_{2}
in the strong coupling
(and nonperturbative) regime, transforming on the first ”preliminary”
stage such bilinear
operators
of heavy quark fields with masses

\sim\Lambda_{2}
into the appropriate
operators
of light fields, i.e.

\mu_{\rm x}a_{1}
and bilinear
operators
of light
SU({\overline{N}}_{c})
quarks and
dyons, see (
41.1.20
) and (
41.1.22
)-(
41.1.25
)
below;   b) finally, from formed at lower energies
\mu\ll\Lambda_{2}
the
genuine condensates of these light higgsed fields.

In connection with (
41.1.17
)-(
41.1.19
), it is worth
asking the following question. Because the difference between

{\mathcal{W}}_{\rm matter}
in (
40.1
) and
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{\rm matter}

in (
41.1.2
) and formation of dyons originate from the color
symmetry breaking
SU(N_{c})\rightarrow SU({\overline{N}}_{c})\times U^{2N_{c}-N_{F}}(1)
in the high
energy region
\mu\sim\Lambda_{2}
, how e.g. the condensate

\langle\Sigma_{D}\rangle
of these dyons knows about the number
{\rm n}_{1}

which originates only at much lower energies
\sim m
? (Put attention
that even the leading term
\sim\mu_{\rm x}m
of
\langle\Sigma_{D}\rangle
in
(
41.1.20
) is much smaller than the separate terms
\langle{\overline{D}}_{j}\rangle\langle D_{j}\rangle\sim\mu_{\rm x}\Lambda_{2}
, but these largest terms

\sim\mu_{\rm x}\Lambda_{2}
cancel in the sum over j). And similarly, because the
color breaking
\langle X^{adj}_{SU(2N_{c}-N_{F})}\rangle\sim\Lambda_{2}
does not
break
by itself
the global flavor symmetry, why the condensates
of heavy quarks
\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{2N_{c}-N_{F}}
and

\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{2N_{c}-N_{F}}
are different ?

First, about
\langle\Sigma_{D}\rangle
. The answer is that, indeed,
both
operators

{\rm Tr\,}(({\overline{Q}}Q))_{2N_{c}-N_{F}}
and
\Sigma_{D}
in
(
41.1.18
) are by themselves
U(N_{F})_{\rm flavor}\times U({\overline{N}}_{c})_{\rm color}
singlets. The explicit dependence on
{\rm n}_{1}
appears
in
\langle\Sigma_{D}\rangle
in (
41.1.19
) (and in left hand
sides of (
41.1.17
),(
41.1.25
),(
41.1.26
))
only   from the
region of much lower energies, after taking the corresponding   total
vacuum averages
\langle...\rangle
in br2 vacua
. Recall that
in these vacua. -

1)
\langle a_{1}\rangle\sim m
is higgsed and operates at the scale

\sim m
. It ”eats” the masses
"m"
of all dyons in (
41.1.5
),
as otherwise all these dyons would not be higgsed but would decouple
as heavy at
\mu<m
.
2N_{c}-N_{F}
unequal double roots of the curve
(
40.2
) will be absent, while
2N_{c}-N_{F}
photons will remain
massless.

2)
SU({\overline{N}}_{c})
is broken also at the scale
\sim m
to
SU({\rm n}_{1})\times U^{(2)}(1)\times SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})
by higgsed
\langle X^{adj}_{SU({\overline{N}}_{c})}\rangle\sim\langle a_{2}\rangle\sim m
. Together with

\langle a_{1}\rangle
, this
\langle a_{2}\rangle
”eats” masses ”m” of
all
SU({\rm n}_{1})
quarks,
\langle(m-a_{1}-a_{2})\rangle=0
. Otherwise all
these
SU({\rm n}_{1})
quarks will decouple as heavy at
\mu<m
, there will
remain
SU({\rm n}_{1})\,\,{\cal N}=2
SYM at
\mu<m
, the multiplicity will
be wrong and
U(N_{F})
will remain unbroken. At the same time, all
quarks in the
SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})
color sector remain with masses
\sim m
.

3) To avoid
g^{2}(\mu<\langle\Lambda^{SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle)<0
in
{\cal N}=2\,\,SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})
SYM,
\langle X^{adj}_{SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}\rangle
is higgsed at the scale

\sim\langle\Lambda^{SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle,\,\,SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})\rightarrow U^{{\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}-1}(1)
.

The (non-factorizable) mean values
\langle(({\overline{Q}}Q))_{1,2}\rangle_{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}
of quarks with masses
\sim m
in the

SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})
SYM sector are determined by the one-loop Konishi
anomaly for these massive non-higgsed quarks. I.e., on the first
”preliminary”
operator stage
at the scale
\mu\sim m
in the
weak coupling regime, the one-loop diagrams with scalar quarks and
their fermionic superpartners with masses
\sim m
inside, transform
the quark
operators

[\,(({\overline{Q}}Q))_{1,2}\,]_{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}
into the
operator

S_{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}/m
of lighter
SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})
gluino (plus
operators with total superderivatives). And the mean value of

\langle S\rangle_{{\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}=\widetilde{\mu}_{\rm x}\langle\Lambda^{SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle^{2}
, originates only at much lower energies

\mu\lesssim\langle\Lambda^{SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle
,
see (
41.1.7
).

4) The global
U(N_{F})
symmetry is broken to
U({\rm n}_{1})\times U({\rm n}_{2})
by
higgsed quarks in the
SU({\rm n}_{1})
color sector at the scale
\sim(\mu_{\rm x}m)^{1/2}\ll\langle\Lambda^{SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle\ll m\ll\Lambda_{2}
.

5) The
total mean value
of
\langle\Sigma_{D}\rangle\sim\mu_{\rm x}m\ll\mu_{\rm x}\Lambda_{2}
in (
41.1.19
) originates and saturates at energies

\sim(\mu_{\rm x}\Lambda_{2})^{1/2}
. The reason is that
D_{j}
and
{\overline{D}}_{j}

move independently
in the whole energy interval
[\mu^{\rm low}_{\rm cut}=(\mu_{\rm x}\Lambda_{2})^{1/2}]<\mu<\Lambda_{2}/(\rm several)
, and their physical
(i.e. path-dependent) phases induced by interactions with the light

U^{2N_{c}-N_{F}-1}(1)\times U^{(1)}(1)
photons
still fluctuate freely
,
so that
\langle{\overline{D}}_{j}\rangle_{\mu^{\rm low}_{\rm cut}}=\langle D_{j}\rangle_{\mu^{\rm low}_{\rm cut}}=\langle\Sigma_{D}\rangle_{\mu^{\rm low}_{\rm cut}}=0
. The nonzero mean values of all light dyon fields
are formed only at the lower scale
\sim(\mu_{\rm x}\Lambda_{2})^{1/2}\ll\Lambda_{2}
, in the
weak coupling regime, from
coherent condensates
of these
higgsed light dyons
(resulting in the appearance of
\sim(\mu_{\rm x}\Lambda_{2})^{1/2}
masses of all
U^{2N_{c}-N_{F}-1}(1)\times U^{(1)}(1)\,\,{\cal N}=2
multiplets):
\langle{\overline{D}}_{j}D_{j}\rangle=\langle{\overline{D}}_{j}\rangle\langle D_{j}\rangle=-\mu_{\rm x}\Lambda_{2}\,\omega^{j-1}+(2N_{c}-N_{F})^{-1}\langle\Sigma_{D}\rangle
, see
(
41.1.6
),(
AX.8
),(
AX.15
), and (
41.1.19
)
for
\langle\Sigma_{D}\rangle
. (Clearly, the leading terms
\langle D_{j}\rangle=\langle{\overline{D}}_{j}\rangle\sim(\mu_{\rm x}\Lambda_{2})^{1/2},\,j=1...2N_{c}-N_{F}

cancel in
\langle\Sigma_{D}\rangle
).

All particles
in this dyonic sector acquire masses
\sim(\mu_{\rm x}\Lambda_{2})^{1/2}
and decouple as heavy at lower energies. No
lighter particles at all remain in this sector at lower energies.

The number
{\rm n}_{1}
penetrates then into
\langle\Sigma_{D}\rangle
from
the
numerical
relation between the
total vacuum averages

in br2 vacua following from (
41.1.5
),(
41.1.12
) (see
also(
41.4.6
),(
AW.23
),(
AX.4
))

\displaystyle\langle\frac{\partial{\mathcal{W}}^{\,\rm low}_{\rm tot}}{\partial a_{1}}\rangle=0\rightarrow\frac{{\overline{N}}_{c}}{2N_{c}-N_{F}}\langle\Sigma_{D}\rangle=\langle{\rm Tr\,}({\overline{Q}}Q)\rangle_{{\overline{N}}_{c}}-\Bigl[\langle\frac{\partial{\mathcal{W}}_{\rm a_{1}}}{\partial a_{1}}\rangle=-\mu_{\rm x}\frac{{\overline{N}}_{c}N_{F}N_{c}}{(2N_{c}-N_{F})^{2}}\langle a_{1}\rangle\,\Bigr]\,,

(41.1.21)

\langle{\rm Tr\,}({\overline{Q}}Q)\rangle_{{\overline{N}}_{c}}=\langle{\rm Tr\,}({\overline{Q}}Q)\rangle_{{\rm n}_{1}}+\langle{\rm Tr\,}({\overline{Q}}Q)\rangle_{{\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\langle{\rm Tr\,}({\overline{Q}}Q)\rangle_{{\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}=-\langle\frac{\partial{\mathcal{W}}^{(SYM)}_{SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}}{\partial a_{1}}\rangle\,,

\langle\Sigma_{D}\rangle\approx({\rm n}_{2}-{\rm n}_{1})\mu_{\rm x}\,\hat{m}\Biggl[1+\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{{\rm n}_{2}-N_{c}}\Bigl(\frac{\hat{m}}{\Lambda_{2}}\Bigr)^{\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{{\rm n}_{2}-N_{c}}}\Biggr)\Biggr]\,,

because all
N_{F}
flavors of quarks from the color
SU({\overline{N}}_{c})
and all
dyons interact with
a_{1}
. (
41.1.21
) agrees with
(
41.1.17
)-(
41.1.19
).
Remind that, by definition,
\langle O\rangle
means the total mean
value of any operator
O
integrated from very high energies down to

\mu^{\rm lowest}_{\rm cut}=0
. The equation (
41.1.21
) is the
example of
numerical
relations between total mean values where
each term originates and saturates at different energies.

Now, about
\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{2N_{c}-N_{F}}
and
\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{2N_{c}-N_{F}}
.
The heavy quark operators in (
41.1.17
) include both the

SU(N_{F})
adjoint and singlet in flavor parts. As for the singlet
part, its mean value is given in (
41.1.18
),(
41.1.19
).
As   for the adjoint parts, they look as, see (
41.1.18
)

\langle{\overline{Q}}_{j}Q^{i}-\delta^{i}_{j}\frac{1}{N_{F}}{\rm Tr}\,(({\overline{Q}}Q)\rangle_{2N_{c}-N_{F}}=A\langle{\overline{Q}}_{j}Q^{i}-\delta^{i}_{j}\frac{1}{N_{F}}{\rm Tr\,}({\overline{Q}}Q)\rangle_{{\overline{N}}_{c}}\,,

\displaystyle\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}-\frac{1}{N_{F}}{\rm Tr\,}({\overline{Q}}Q)\rangle_{2N_{c}-N_{F}}=A\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}-\frac{1}{N_{F}}{\rm Tr\,}({\overline{Q}}Q)\rangle_{{\overline{N}}_{c}},

(41.1.22)

\displaystyle\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{2N_{c}-N_{F}}=A\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}-\frac{1}{N_{F}}{\rm Tr\,}({\overline{Q}}Q)\rangle_{{\overline{N}}_{c}}+\frac{1}{N_{F}}\langle\Sigma_{D}\rangle.

(41.1.23)

\displaystyle\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}-\frac{1}{N_{F}}{\rm Tr\,}({\overline{Q}}Q)\rangle_{2N_{c}-N_{F}}=A\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}-\frac{1}{N_{F}}{\rm Tr\,}({\overline{Q}}Q)\rangle_{{\overline{N}}_{c}}

(41.1.24)

\displaystyle\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{2N_{c}-N_{F}}=A\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}-\frac{1}{N_{F}}{\rm Tr\,}({\overline{Q}}Q)\rangle_{{\overline{N}}_{c}}+\frac{1}{N_{F}}\langle\Sigma_{D}\rangle\,,

(41.1.25)

where
A=O(1)
is some constant originating from integrating out the
loop (and nonperturbative) effects from the Lagrangian (
40.1
)
over the energy region
\mu\sim\Lambda_{2}
. It is seen from
(
41.1.22
),(
41.1.24
),

(
41.4.1
),(
41.4.6
) that
nonzero contributions to the ”non-genuine” (i.e. non-factorizable)
adjoint in
U(N_{F})
bilinear condensates of heavy non-higgsed quarks
are induced in two stages. a) On the first ”preliminary” operator
stage from the energy range
\Lambda_{2}/(\rm several)<\mu<(\rm several)\Lambda_{2}
, transforming the heavy quarks
operators
in left parts of
(
41.1.22
),(
41.1.24
) into
operators
of light
SU({\overline{N}}_{c})

quarks, this is the operator expansion. b) Finally, at the second
numerical stage at lower energies
\sim(\mu_{\rm x}m)^{1/2}
, by the
”genuine” (i.e. coherent) factorizable condensate

\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{{\rm n}_{1}}=\langle{\overline{Q}}^{1}_{1}\rangle\langle Q^{1}_{1}\rangle\approx\,\hat{m}\mu_{\rm x}
(
41.1.14
) of
light higgsed quarks which break spontaneously
U(N_{F})\rightarrow U({\rm n}_{1})\times U({\rm n}_{2})
.   Unfortunately, we cannot calculate directly the coefficient ’A’
originating from the scale
\mu\sim\Lambda_{2}
, but we can claim that it does
not know the number
{\rm n}_{1}
appearing only at much lower scale
\mu\sim m\ll\Lambda_{2}
. Besides, we can find ’A’ e.g. from (
41.1.23
) using
(
41.4.1
),(
41.4.6
),(
41.1.19
) for the right hand
part, and then predict
\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{2N_{c}-N_{F}}
in (
41.1.25
).
As a result,

\displaystyle A=-2\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{2N_{c}-N_{F}}\approx-\mu_{\rm x}\,\hat{m}\Biggl[1+\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{{\rm n}_{2}-N_{c}}\Bigl(\frac{\hat{m}}{\Lambda_{2}}\Bigr)^{\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{{\rm n}_{2}-N_{c}}}\Biggr]\,,

(41.1.26)

this agrees with (
41.1.17
), and
A=\,-2
is independent of

{\rm n}_{1}
as it should be.

On the whole, the total decomposition of quark condensates
\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1,2}\rangle_{N_{c}}
over their separate color parts look in
these br2 vacua as follows.

I) The condensate
\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{N_{c}}
.

a) From (
41.1.14
), the (factorizable) condensate of higgsed
quarks in the
SU({\rm n}_{1})
part

\displaystyle\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{{\rm n}_{1}}=\langle{\overline{Q}}^{1}_{1}\rangle\langle Q^{1}_{1}\rangle\approx\mu_{\rm x}\hat{m}\Bigl[\,1+\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{{\rm n}_{2}-N_{c}}\Bigl(\frac{\hat{m}}{\Lambda_{2}}\Bigr)^{\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{{\rm n}_{2}-N_{c}}}\,\Bigr]\,.

(41.1.27)

b) The (non-factorizable) condensate
\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{{\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}
is
determined by the one-loop Konishi anomaly for the heavy not higgsed
quarks with the mass
m_{2}
in the
SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})
SYM sector, see
(
41.1.4
),(
41.1.15
)

\displaystyle\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{{\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}=\frac{\langle S\rangle_{{\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}}{m_{2}}\approx\frac{\widetilde{\mu}_{\rm x}\langle\Lambda^{SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle^{2}}{m_{2}}\approx\mu_{\rm x}\hat{m}\Bigl(\frac{\hat{m}}{\Lambda_{2}}\Bigr)^{\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{{\rm n}_{2}-N_{c}}}\,.

(41.1.28)

c) From (
41.1.17
), the (non-factorizable) condensate of
heavy non-higgsed quarks with masses
\sim\Lambda_{2}

\displaystyle\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{2N_{c}-N_{F}}\approx-\mu_{\rm x}\,\hat{m}\Biggl[1+\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{{\rm n}_{2}-N_{c}}\Bigl(\frac{\hat{m}}{\Lambda_{2}}\Bigr)^{\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{{\rm n}_{2}-N_{c}}}\Biggr]\,.

(41.1.29)

Therefore, on the whole

\displaystyle\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{N_{c}}=\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{{\rm n}_{1}}+\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{{\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}+\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{2N_{c}-N_{F}}\approx\mu_{\rm x}\,\hat{m}\Bigl(\frac{\hat{m}}{\Lambda_{2}}\Bigr)^{\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{{\rm n}_{2}-N_{c}}}\,,\,\,\hskip 11.74988pt

(41.1.30)

as it should be, see (
41.1
).

II) The condensate
\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{N_{c}}
.

a) From (
41.1.6
) the (factorizable) condensate of the
non-higgsed massless Nambu-Goldstone particles in the

SU({\rm n}_{1})
part

\displaystyle\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{{\rm n}_{1}}=\sum_{a=1}^{{\rm n}_{1}}\langle{\overline{Q}}^{\,a}_{2}\rangle\langle Q^{2}_{a}\rangle=0\,.

(41.1.31)

b) The (non-factorizable) condensate
\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{{\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}

is determined by the same one-loop Konishi anomaly for the heavy
non-higgsed quarks with the mass
m_{2}
in the
SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})
SYM
sector,

\displaystyle\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{{\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}=\frac{\langle S\rangle_{{\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}}{m_{2}}\approx\mu_{\rm x}\,\hat{m}\Bigl(\frac{\hat{m}}{\Lambda_{2}}\Bigr)^{\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{{\rm n}_{2}-N_{c}}}\,.

(41.1.32)

c) From (
41.1.19
), the (non-factorizable) condensate of
heavy non-higgsed quarks with masses
\sim\Lambda_{2}

\displaystyle\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{2N_{c}-N_{F}}\approx\mu_{\rm x}\,\hat{m}\Biggl[1+\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{{\rm n}_{2}-N_{c}}\Bigl(\frac{\hat{m}}{\Lambda_{2}}\Bigr)^{\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{{\rm n}_{2}-N_{c}}}\Biggr]\,.

(41.1.33)

Therefore, on the whole

\displaystyle\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{N_{c}}=\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{{\rm n}_{1}}+\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{{\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}+\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{2N_{c}-N_{F}}\approx\mu_{\rm x}\,\hat{m}\Biggl[1+\frac{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}{{\rm n}_{2}-N_{c}}\Bigl(\frac{\hat{m}}{\Lambda_{2}}\Bigr)^{\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{{\rm n}_{2}-N_{c}}}\Biggr],\,\,\,\hskip 11.74988pt

(41.1.34)

as it should be, see (
41.1
).

And finally, remind that the mass spectra in these br2 vacua depend
essentially on the value of
m/\Lambda_{2}
and all these vacua with
{\rm n}_{2}>N_{c}

evolve at
m\gg\Lambda_{2}
to br1 vacua of section 4.3 below, see e.g.
section 4 in
[
17
]
.

41.2

U(N_{c})
,  smallest
\mu_{\rm x}

U(N_{c})
theory is obtained by adding one
SU(N_{c})
singlet

U^{(0)}(1)

{\cal N}=2
multiplet, with its scalar field
\sqrt{2}X^{(0)}=a_{0}I
, where

is the unit
N_{c}\times N_{c}
matrix.
Instead of (
40.1
), the superpotential looks now as

\displaystyle{\cal W}_{\rm matter}=\frac{\mu_{0}}{2}N_{c}a^{2}_{0}+\mu_{\rm x}{\rm Tr}\,(X^{\rm adj}_{SU(N_{c})})^{2}+{\rm Tr}\,\Bigl[(m-a_{0})\,{\overline{Q}}Q-{\overline{Q}}\sqrt{2}X^{\rm adj}_{SU(N_{c})}Q\Bigr]_{N_{c}}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\mu_{0}=\mu_{\rm x}\,,

(41.2.1)

and we consider in this paper only the case with

. The only

) and in the value of

\displaystyle\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}+({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{N_{c}}=\mu_{\rm x}\,m\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{N_{c}}\approx\mu_{\rm x}\,m\gg\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{N_{c}}\approx\mu_{\rm x}\,m\Bigl(\frac{m}{\Lambda_{2}}\Bigr)^{\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{{\rm n}_{2}-N_{c}}},

(41.2.2)

\langle S\rangle_{N_{c}}=\frac{\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{N_{c}}\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{N_{c}}}{\mu_{\rm x}}\approx\mu_{\rm x}\,m^{2}\Bigl(\frac{m}{\Lambda_{2}}\Bigr)^{\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{{\rm n}_{2}-N_{c}}},

while in br2 vacua the small ratio
\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{N_{c}}/\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{N_{c}}\ll 1
and small
\langle S\rangle_{N_{c}}\ll\mu_{\rm x}\,m^{2}
in (

) remain parametrically the
same. Besides, from (

),(

) (neglecting power

\displaystyle\langle a_{0}\rangle=\frac{\langle\,{\rm Tr}\,(({\overline{Q}}Q))\rangle_{N_{c}}}{N_{c}\mu_{\rm x}}=\frac{{\rm n}_{1}\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{N_{c}}+{\rm n}_{2}\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{N_{c}}}{N_{c}\mu_{\rm x}}\approx\frac{{\rm n}_{2}}{N_{c}}\,m,\,\,\langle m-a_{0}\rangle\approx-\,\frac{{\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}{N_{c}}\,m.\,\,\,\,

(41.2.3)

The changes in (
41.1.2
) and (
41.1.5
) are also very
simple : a)  the term ”
\mu_{\rm x}N_{c}a^{2}_{0}/2
”  is added,   b)
”
m

m-a_{0}
” in all other terms. So, instead of

),(

\displaystyle{\mathcal{W}}^{\,\rm low}_{\rm tot}={\mathcal{W}}^{\,(SYM)}_{SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}+{\mathcal{W}}^{\,\rm low}_{\rm matter}+\dots\,,\hskip 11.74988pt{\mathcal{W}}^{\,\rm low}_{\rm matter}={\mathcal{W}}_{SU({\rm n}_{1})}+{\mathcal{W}}_{D}+{\mathcal{W}}_{a}\,,

(41.2.4)

{\mathcal{W}}^{\,(SYM)}_{SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}=({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})\,\widetilde{\mu}_{\rm x}\Bigl(\Lambda^{SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\Bigr)^{2}+{\mathcal{W}}^{\,(M)}_{SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\langle\Lambda^{SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle^{2}\approx m^{2}\Bigl(\frac{m}{\Lambda_{2}}\Bigr)^{\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{{\rm n}_{2}-N_{c}}}\,,

{\mathcal{W}}_{SU({\rm n}_{1})}=(m-a_{0}-a_{1}-a_{2})\,{\rm Tr}\,({\overline{Q}}Q)_{{\rm n}_{1}}-\,{\rm Tr}\,({\overline{Q}}\sqrt{2}X^{\rm adj}_{SU({\rm n}_{1})}Q)_{{\rm n}_{1}}+\widetilde{\mu}_{\rm x}{\rm Tr}\,(X^{\rm adj}_{SU({\rm n}_{1})})^{2}\,,

{\mathcal{W}}_{D}=(m-a_{0}-c_{1}a_{1})\sum_{j=1}^{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{\overline{D}}_{j}D_{j}-\sum_{j=1}^{2N_{c}-N_{F}}a_{D,j}\,{\overline{D}}_{j}D_{j}\,-\,\mu_{\rm x}\Lambda_{2}\sum_{j=1}^{2N_{c}-N_{F}}\omega^{j-1}\,a_{D,j}+\mu_{\rm x}L\sum_{j=1}^{2N_{c}-N_{F}}a_{D,j}\,,

{\mathcal{W}}_{a}=\frac{\mu_{\rm x}}{2}\Biggl[N_{c}a^{2}_{0}+(1+\delta_{1})\frac{{\overline{N}}_{c}N_{c}}{{\rm b}_{2}}a_{1}^{2}+(1+\delta_{2})\frac{{\rm n}_{1}{\overline{N}}_{c}}{{\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}a_{2}^{2}+2N_{c}\delta_{3}a_{1}(m-a_{0}-c_{1}a_{1})+2N_{c}\delta_{4}(m-a_{0}-c_{1}a_{1})^{2}\Biggr].

From (
41.2.3
),(
41.2.4
) (the leading terms only)

\displaystyle\langle a_{1}\rangle=-\,\frac{{\rm b}_{2}}{{\overline{N}}_{c}}\langle m-a_{0}\rangle\approx\frac{{\rm b}_{2}({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}{{\overline{N}}_{c}N_{c}}\,m\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\langle a_{2}\rangle=\langle m-a_{0}-a_{1}\rangle\approx-\,\frac{{\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}{{\overline{N}}_{c}}\,m\,,

(41.2.5)

while, according to reasonings given in section 41.1, all
\delta_{i}

should remain the same. Of course, this can be checked directly.
Instead of (
41.1.8
) we have now for the leading terms

\displaystyle\langle{\mathcal{W}}^{\,\rm low}_{\rm tot}\rangle\approx\langle{\mathcal{W}}^{\,\rm low}_{\rm matter}\rangle\approx\frac{\mu_{\rm x}}{2}\Biggl[\,N_{c}\langle a_{0}\rangle^{2}+\frac{{\overline{N}}_{c}N_{c}}{{\rm b}_{2}}(1+\delta_{1})\langle a_{1}\rangle^{2}+\frac{{\rm n}_{1}{\overline{N}}_{c}}{{\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}(1+\delta_{2})\langle a_{2}\rangle^{2}\,\Biggr]\,,

(41.2.6)

where
\langle a_{i}\rangle
are given in  (
41.2.3
),(
41.2.5
).
Instead of   (
41.1.9
),(
41.1.10
)
we have now (with the same accuracy)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{W}}^{\,\rm eff}_{\rm tot}(\Pi)=m\,{\rm Tr}\,({\overline{Q}}Q)_{N_{c}}-\frac{1}{2\mu_{\rm x}}\Biggl[\,\sum_{i,j=1}^{N_{F}}({\overline{Q}}_{j}Q^{i})_{N_{c}}({\overline{Q}}_{\,i}Q^{j})_{N_{c}}\Biggr]-{\overline{N}}_{c}S_{N_{c}}\rightarrow\langle{\mathcal{W}}^{\,\rm eff}_{\rm tot}\rangle\approx\frac{1}{2}\,{\rm n}_{2}\,\mu_{\rm x}m^{2}.\,\,\,

(41.2.7)

It is not difficult to check that values of
\delta_{1,2}
obtained
from (
41.2.6
)=(
41.2.7
) are the same as in
(
41.1.12
).

54

54

Besides, one can check that (
41.2.4
) with all

\delta_{i}=0,\,i=1...4
, will result in the wrong value:
\langle a_{0}\rangle\approx[\,m\,(2N_{c}-{\rm n}_{2})/N_{c}\,]
, this differs from
(
41.2.3
).

Therefore, instead of (
41.1.13
), we obtain now finally for the
leading term, see (
41.2.4
),(
41.2.5
),

\langle{\rm Tr}\,({\overline{Q}}Q)\rangle_{{\rm n}_{1}}={\rm n}_{1}\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{{\rm n}_{1}}+{\rm n}_{2}\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{{\rm n}_{1}}={\rm n}_{1}\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{{\rm n}_{1}}\approx\widetilde{\mu}_{\rm x}\,\frac{{\rm n}_{1}{\overline{N}}_{c}}{{\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}\langle a_{2}\rangle\approx{\rm n}_{1}\mu_{\rm x}m\,,

\displaystyle\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle^{U(N_{c})}_{{\rm n}_{1}}=\sum_{a=1}^{{\rm n}_{1}}\langle{\overline{Q}}_{1}^{\,a}Q^{1}_{a}\rangle=\langle{\overline{Q}}^{1}_{1}\rangle\langle Q^{1}_{1}\rangle\approx\mu_{\rm x}m\approx\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle^{U(N_{c})}_{N_{c}},\,\,\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{{\rm n}_{1}}=0,\,\,\langle S\rangle_{{\rm n}_{1}}=0,\,\,\,\,\,

(41.2.8)

while as before in
SU(N_{c})

\displaystyle\langle{\overline{D}}_{j}\rangle\langle D_{j}\rangle=-\mu_{\rm x}\Lambda_{2}\omega^{j-1}+O(\mu_{\rm x}\,m)\,.

(41.2.9)

It is worth to remind that the number of double roots of the curve
(
40.2
) in this case is still
N_{c}-1
, as two single roots with

e^{+}=-e^{-}\approx 2\langle\Lambda^{SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle
still originate here from
SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})\,\,{\cal N}=2

SYM.   In other words, the number of charged particles massless at

\mu_{\rm x}\rightarrow 0
(and higgsed at small
\mu_{\rm x}\neq 0
) remains
N_{c}-1
as in
the
SU(N_{c})
theory. But now, in comparison with the
SU(N_{c})

theory, one extra
U^{(0)}(1)
gauge multiplet is added. As a result,
in addition to
2{\rm n}_{1}{\rm n}_{2}
massless Nambu-Goldstone multiplets, there
remains now one exactly massless
{\cal N}=1
photon multiplet, while
the corresponding scalar
{\cal N}=1
multiplet has now smallest
nonzero mass
\sim\mu_{\rm x}
due to breaking
{\cal N}=2\rightarrow{\cal N}=1
.

In so far as we know from (
41.1.5
),(
41.2.4
) the charges
and numbers of all particles massless at
\mu_{\rm x}\rightarrow 0
, we can establish
the definite correspondence with the roots of the curve   (
40.2
):

2N_{c}-N_{F}
unequal double roots
e_{j}\sim\Lambda_{2},\,j=1...2N_{c}-N_{F}

correspond to our dyons
D_{j}
,
{\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}-1
unequal double roots

e_{i}\sim\langle\Lambda^{SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle,\,i=1...N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}-1
correspond to pure magnetic monopoles
from
SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})
SYM, and
{\rm n}_{1}
equal double roots
e_{k}\sim m,\,k=1...{\rm n}_{1}
correspond to
{\rm n}_{1}
higgsed original pure electric quarks
from
SU({\rm n}_{1})
. Besides, we know from (
41.2.8
),(
41.2.9
)
the leading terms of their condensates (see (
41.1.6
) for the
monopole condensates). Then, as a check, we can compare the values of
condensates in (
41.2.8
),(
41.2.9
) with the formulas
proposed in
[
44
]
where these condensates are expressed through
the roots of the
U(N_{c})
curve (
40.2
).

55
55

55

Remind that these formulas were derived in
[
44
]
from and checked
then on a   few simplest examples only, and proposed after this to be
universal.
Besides, in general, the main problem with the use of the expressions
like (
41.2.10
) from
[
44
]
for finding the condensates of
definite light BPS particles (massless at
\mu_{\rm x}\rightarrow 0
) is that one
needs to find first the values of all roots of the curve
(
40.2
). But for this, in practice, one has to understand, at
least, the main properties of the color and flavor symmetry breaking
in different vacua, the corresponding mass hierarchies, and
multiplicities of each type roots.

Specifically, these roots look in these
U(N_{c})
br2 vacua as:  1) the
quark double roots
e^{(Q)}_{k}=-m,\,\,k=1...{\rm n}_{1}
,   2) the dyon
double roots
e^{(D)}_{j}\approx\omega^{j-1}\Lambda_{2},\,\,j=1...(2N_{c}-N_{F})
,   3) two single
roots
[
31
,
45
,
46
]
which we know originate from
SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})\,\,{\cal N}=2
SYM,
e^{+}=-e^{-}\approx 2\langle\Lambda^{SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle
. Therefore, with this knowledge,
the formulas from
[
44
]
look as

\displaystyle\langle{\overline{Q}}_{k}Q^{k}\rangle^{U(N_{c})}_{{\rm n}_{1}}=-\mu_{\rm x}\sqrt{(e^{(Q)}_{k}-e^{+})(e^{(Q)}_{k}-e^{-})}\approx-\mu_{\rm x}e^{(Q)}_{k}\approx\mu_{\rm x}m\,,\hskip 11.74988ptk=1...{\rm n}_{1},

(41.2.10)

\langle{\overline{D}}_{j}D_{j}\rangle=-\mu_{\rm x}\sqrt{(e^{(D)}_{j}-e^{+})(e^{(D)}_{j}-e^{-})}\approx-\mu_{\rm x}e^{(D)}_{j}\approx\,-\mu_{\rm x}\omega^{j-1}\Lambda_{2}\,,\hskip 11.74988ptj=1...2N_{c}-N_{F}\,.

( Both
e^{\pm}
are neglected here because they are non-leading). It
is seen that the leading terms of (
41.2.8
),(
41.2.9
) and
(
41.2.10
) agree, this is non-trivial as the results were
obtained very different methods. The calculations of power corrections
to (
41.2.8
),(
41.2.9
), and (
41.2.10
) are presented
in important   Appendices A and B.

41.3

SU(N_{c})
,  larger

\mu_{\rm x}\,,\,\,\,\langle\Lambda^{SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle\ll\mu_{\rm x}\ll\,m

Significant changes for such values of
\mu_{\rm x}
occurs only in the
{\cal N}=2\,\,SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})
SYM sector. All
SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})
adjoint scalars

X^{adj}_{SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}
are
too heavy and too short ranged
now,
their physical (i.e. path dependent)
SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})
phases induced by
interactions with the lighter
SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})
gluons fluctuate freely at
all scales
\mu\gtrsim\langle\Lambda^{SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=1\,\,SYM}\rangle
in this case, and they are not higgsed, i.e.

\langle X^{adj}_{SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}\rangle_{\mu_{\rm x}^{\rm pole}/(\rm several)}=0
. Instead, they all decouple as heavy already at the
scale
\langle\Lambda^{SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=1\,\,SYM}\rangle\ll\mu<[\mu_{\rm x}^{\rm pole}={\it g}^{2}\mu_{\rm x}]/(\rm several)\ll m

in the weak coupling region
and do not affect
then by themselves the lower energy dynamics. There remains
{\cal N}=1\,\,SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})
SYM with the scale factor of its gauge coupling

\langle\Lambda^{SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=1\,SYM}\rangle=[\,\widetilde{\mu}_{\rm x}\langle\Lambda^{SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle^{2}\,]^{1/3},\\
\langle\Lambda^{SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle\ll\langle\Lambda^{SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=1\,\,SYM}\rangle\ll\mu_{\rm x}\ll\penalty\ m
,   see (
41.1.4
),(
41.1.15
). The small nonzero
(non-factorizable) value
\langle{\,\rm Tr\,}(X^{adj}_{SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})})^{2}\rangle=({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})\langle S\rangle_{{\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}/\widetilde{\mu}_{\rm x}=({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})\langle\Lambda^{SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle^{2}\ll\langle\Lambda^{SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=1\,\,SYM}\rangle^{2}
arises
here not because
X^{adj}_{SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}
are higgsed, but only due to
the Konishi anomaly. I.e., on the first ”preliminary” operator stage,
from one-loop diagrams with heavy scalars
X^{adj}_{SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}
and
their fermionic superpartners with masses
\sim\mu_{\rm x}
inside,
transforming (in the weak coupling regime at the scale
\sim\mu_{\rm x}
) this

operator

{\,\rm Tr\,}(X^{adj}_{SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})})^{2}
of heavy
scalars into the bilinear
operator

\sim({\rm Tr\,}\lambda\lambda)_{{\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}/\mu_{\rm x}
of lighter gluinos of
{\cal N}=1\,\,SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})
SYM. But the mean vacuum value of this latter
originates and saturates only in the strong coupling (and
non-perturbative) region at much smaller energies

\mu\sim\langle\Lambda^{SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=1\,\,SYM}\rangle\ll\mu_{\rm x}
.

The multiplicity of vacua of this
{\cal N}=1\,\,SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})
SYM is
also
{\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}
, as it should be.. There appears now a large number
of strongly coupled
{\cal N}=1
gluonia with the mass scale

\sim\langle\Lambda^{SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=1\,\,SYM}\rangle
. All
heavier
SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})
charged original pure electric quarks and
hybrids with masses
\sim m
and scalars
X^{adj}_{SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}
with
masses
\sim\mu_{\rm x}\gg\langle\Lambda^{SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=1\,\,SYM}\rangle
are still weakly confined, but the tension of the
confining string is larger now,

\sigma^{1/2}_{SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}\sim\langle\Lambda^{SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=1\,\,SYM}\rangle\ll\mu_{\rm x}\ll m
.

At the same time, in this range
\langle\Lambda^{SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=1\,\,SYM}\rangle\ll\mu_{\rm x}\ll m
, nothing has happened yet with

\langle a_{2}\rangle\sim m

still higgsed
at the scale
\mu\sim m\gg\mu_{\rm x}
, resulting in
SU({\overline{N}}_{c})\rightarrow SU({\rm n}_{1})\times U^{(2)}(1)\times SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})
, and in the
SU({\rm n}_{1})
sector with its quarks higgsed at

\mu\sim(\mu_{\rm x}m)^{1/2}\gg\mu_{\rm x}
.

41.4

SU(N_{c})
,   even larger
\mu_{\rm x}\,,\,\,\,m\ll\mu_{\rm x}\ll\Lambda_{2}

The case with
m\ll\mu_{\rm x}\ll\Lambda_{2}
and
N_{c}+1<N_{F}<3N_{c}/2
is
described in section 8.1 of
[
25
]
. Therefore, we consider here in
addition the region
3N_{c}/2<N_{F}<2N_{c}-1
.

Remind, that the whole dyonic
{\rm b}_{2}=2N_{c}-N_{F}
sector, including the

U^{(1)}(1)\,\,{\cal N}=2
photon multiplet with its scalar partner

a_{1}
, acquires masses
\sim(\mu_{\rm x}\Lambda_{2})^{1/2}\gg\mu_{\rm x}
and decouples at
lower energies. But finally, the trace of the heavier scalar
a_{1}

remains in the lower energy
{\cal W}_{SU({\overline{N}}_{c})}
superpotential at
scales
\mu<(\mu_{\rm x}\Lambda_{2})^{1/2},\,\,\mu_{\rm x}\ll(\mu_{\rm x}\Lambda_{2})^{1/2}\ll\Lambda_{2}
, in the
form:
\,m\rightarrow{\widetilde{m}}=(m-\langle a_{1}\rangle)=mN_{c}/{\overline{N}}_{c}\,
.
Remind also   that, see (
41.1.2
),(
41.1.12
),

\mu_{\rm x}\rightarrow\widetilde{\mu}_{\rm x}=(1+\delta_{2})\mu_{\rm x}=-\mu_{\rm x}
.

Therefore, after integrating out all particles with masses
\sim(\mu_{\rm x}\Lambda_{2})^{1/2}
, the lower energy superpotential at the scale
\mu=(\mu_{\rm x}\Lambda_{2})^{1/2}/{(\rm several)}\gg\mu_{\rm x}
looks as, see also
(
41.4.5
),(
41.4.6
),

\displaystyle{\mathcal{W}}^{({\cal N}=2,\,{\overline{N}}_{c})}_{\rm matter}={\rm Tr}\,\Bigl[\,{\overline{Q}}\,(\widetilde{m}-\sqrt{2}X^{\rm adj}_{SU({\overline{N}}_{c})})\,Q\,\Bigr]_{{\overline{N}}_{c}}+\widetilde{\mu}_{\rm x}{\rm Tr}\,(X^{\rm adj}_{SU({\overline{N}}_{c})})^{2},\,\,\widetilde{m}=\frac{N_{c}}{{\overline{N}}_{c}}m,\,\,\widetilde{\mu}_{\rm x}=-\mu_{\rm x}\,,

(41.4.1)

41.4.15
) and all
N_{F}^{2}\,\,\Phi^{j}_{i}
also remain
dynamically irrelevant. The overall hierarchies of
nonzero masses look in this case as

\displaystyle\mu(\Pi^{1}_{1})\ll\Lambda^{SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=1\,\,SYM}\sim{\widehat{m}}_{Q,2}^{\rm pole}\ll\mu_{\rm gl,1}\ll\tilde{\Lambda}\ll\mu_{\rm x}\ll\Lambda_{2}\,.

(41.4.18)

On the whole for this section 41.4 with
m\ll\mu_{\rm x}\ll\Lambda_{2}
. -

In all cases considered the overall phase
\rm\mathbf{Higgs_{1}-HQ_{2}}

(HQ=heavy quark) remains the same in the non-Abelian
SU({\overline{N}}_{c})
sector.
At scales
\mu_{\rm x}\ll\mu\ll\Lambda_{2}
it behaves as the effectively massless IR
free
{\cal N}=2
theory. At the scale
\mu\sim\mu_{\rm x}^{\rm pole}=g^{2}\mu_{\rm x}

all
X^{adj}_{SU({\overline{N}}_{c})}
decouple as heavy and its dynamics becomes
those of the
{\cal N}=1
theory.
2{\rm n}_{1}^{2}
quarks
Q^{1},{\overline{Q}}_{1}

with
SU({\rm n}_{1})
colors and
{\rm n}_{1}
flavors are higgsed while
2{\rm n}_{2}({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})
quarks
Q^{2},{\overline{Q}}_{2}
with
SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})
colors and
{\rm n}_{2}

flavors are in the HQ phase and confined by the
{\cal N}=1\,SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})
SYM.
2{\rm n}_{1}{\rm n}_{2}
massless Nambu-Goldstone particles in all
cases are in essence the hybrid quarks with
{\rm n}_{2}
flavors and broken

SU({\rm n}_{1})
colors. And the overall qualitative picture is also the same
in all variants considered, see e.g. the text after (
41.4.11
).
Changes only the character of the
{\cal N}=1
RG evolution, i.e. it
is logarithmic or power-like, and this influences the values of
nonzero masses and mass hierarchies.

Finally, we think it will be useful to make the following short
additional comments.

In this section, the largest masses
\mu_{\rm x}^{\rm pole}=g^{2}(\mu=\mu_{\rm x}^{\rm pole})\mu_{\rm x}\gg(\mu_{\rm x}m)^{1/2}\gg m
in the whole
SU({\overline{N}}_{c})
sector have

{\overline{N}}_{c}^{\,2}-1
adjoint scalars
X^{\rm adj}_{SU({\overline{N}}_{c})}
. They all are

too heavy and too short ranged
now, and in the whole energy
region
\mu>(\rm several)(\mu_{\rm x}m)^{1/2}
at least
their physical (i.e. path dependent)
SU({\overline{N}}_{c})
phases induced by
interactions with the
lighter
(and effectively massless at such
energies)
SU({\overline{N}}_{c})
gluons fluctuate freely. Therefore, the mean
value   of
X^{\rm adj}_{SU({\overline{N}}_{c})}
, integrated not only over the
interval from   the high energy down
to
\mu_{\rm x}^{\rm pole}/({\rm several})
as in the
Lagrangian (
41.4.3
),(
41.4.4
), but even down to much
lower energies
\mu_{\rm cut}^{\rm low}=(\rm several)(\mu_{\rm x}m)^{1/2}\ll\mu_{\rm x}
is zero,

\langle\sqrt{2}X^{\rm adj}_{SU({\overline{N}}_{c})}\rangle_{\mu^{\rm low}_{\rm cut}}=2\sum_{i=1}^{N_{F}}\sum_{A=1}^{{\overline{N}}_{c}^{\,2}-1}T^{A}\langle{\rm Tr\,}{\overline{Q}}_{i}T^{A}Q^{i}\rangle_{\mu^{\rm low}_{\rm cut}}/\widetilde{\mu}_{\rm x}=

\displaystyle=2\sum_{i=1}^{{\rm n}_{1}}\sum_{A=1}^{{\overline{N}}_{c}^{\,2}-1}T^{A}{\rm Tr}\Biggl[\langle{\overline{Q}}_{i}\rangle_{\mu^{\rm low}_{\rm cut}}T^{A}\langle Q^{i}\rangle_{\mu^{\rm low}_{\rm cut}}\Biggr]/\widetilde{\mu}_{\rm x}=0\,,\,

(41.4.19)

because all light quark fields
Q^{i}_{a}
and
{\overline{Q}}^{\,a}_{i},\,a=1...{\overline{N}}_{c},\,i=1...N_{F}
(and all light
SU({\overline{N}}_{c})
gluons)
still
fluctuate independently and freely
in this higher energy region.

But its formal
total mean value

\langle X^{\rm adj}_{SU({\overline{N}}_{c})}\rangle
, integrated by definition down to
\mu^{\rm lowest}_{\rm cut}=0
, contains the small but nonzero,
\sim m\ll(\mu_{\rm x}m)^{1/2}
, contribution originating only in the lower energy region

\mu\sim(\mu_{\rm x}m)^{1/2}\ll\mu_{\rm x}
from the classical coupling of heavy

X^{\rm adj}_{SU({\overline{N}}_{c})}
with
lighter higgsed quarks

{\overline{Q}}^{\,a}_{i},Q^{i}_{a},\,a,i=1...{\rm n}_{1}
, see e.g. (
41.4.1
) or
(
41.1.14
),

\langle\sqrt{2}X^{\rm\,adj}_{SU({\overline{N}}_{c})}\rangle=\langle\sqrt{2}\sum_{A=1}^{{\overline{N}}_{c}^{\,2}-1}X^{A}\,T^{A}\rangle\approx 2\sum_{i=1}^{N_{F}}\sum_{A=1}^{{\overline{N}}_{c}^{\,2}-1}T^{A}\langle{\rm Tr\,}{\overline{Q}}_{i}T^{A}Q^{i}\rangle/\widetilde{\mu}_{\rm x}\approx

\displaystyle\approx 2\sum_{i=1}^{{\rm n}_{1}}\sum_{A=1}^{{\overline{N}}_{c}^{\,2}-1}T^{A}{\rm Tr\,}[\,\langle{\overline{Q}}\rangle_{i}T^{A}\langle Q^{i}\rangle\,]/\widetilde{\mu}_{\rm x}\approx m_{2}\,{\rm diag}\,(\,\underbrace{\,1-\frac{{\rm n}_{1}}{{\overline{N}}_{c}}}_{{\rm n}_{1}}\,;\,\underbrace{\,-\frac{{\rm n}_{1}}{{\overline{N}}_{c}}}_{{\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}\,)\,.

(41.4.20)

This leads to the main contribution
\sim m^{2}
to
{\rm Tr\,}[\langle(\sqrt{2}X^{\rm adj}_{SU({\overline{N}}_{c})})^{2}\rangle]
, which in the case
considered looks as
{\rm Tr\,}[\langle(\sqrt{2}X^{\rm adj}_{SU({\overline{N}}_{c})})^{2}\rangle]\approx{\rm Tr\,}[\langle\sqrt{2}X^{\rm adj}_{SU({\overline{N}}_{c})}\rangle\langle\sqrt{2}X^{\rm adj}_{SU({\overline{N}}_{c})}\rangle]
,   see (
41.4.20
),

\displaystyle{\rm Tr\,}[\langle(\sqrt{2}X^{\rm adj}_{SU({\overline{N}}_{c})})^{2}\rangle]\approx\Bigl[\frac{{\rm n}_{1}({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}{{\overline{N}}_{c}}\,m_{2}^{2}=\frac{{\rm n}_{1}{\overline{N}}_{c}}{{\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}\,\widetilde{m}^{2}\,\Bigr],\,\,\widetilde{m}=\frac{N_{c}}{{\overline{N}}_{c}}m,\,\,m_{2}=\frac{N_{c}}{{\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}m\,,\,\,\,\,

(41.4.21)

this agrees with the exact relation in the last line of
(
41.4.1
) following from the Konishi anomaly (up to small
nonperturbative power corrections
\sim\langle S\rangle_{{\overline{N}}_{c}}/\mu_{\rm x}\sim\hat{m}^{2}(\hat{m}/\Lambda_{2})^{(2N_{c}-N_{F})/({\rm n}_{2}-N_{c})}
originating finally at even lower
energies from the SYM part), and explains the origin of a smooth
holomorphic behavior of
\langle{\rm Tr\,}(\sqrt{2}X^{\rm adj}_{SU({\overline{N}}_{c})})^{2}\rangle
when
\mu_{\rm x}
is increased from

\mu_{\rm x}\ll m
in section 41.1 to
\mu_{\rm x}\gg m
in this section.

But, in the case considered, this small formal nonzero total mean
value of
\langle X^{\rm adj}_{SU({\overline{N}}_{c})}\rangle\sim m\ll(\mu_{\rm x}m)^{1/2}
does not mean that these heavy adjoint
scalars are
higgsed
, in the sense that they give by themselves

the additional contributions

\sim m

to particle masses

(those of hybrid gluons and
SU({\overline{N}}_{c})
quarks in this case), as it
really occurs at
\mu_{\rm x}\ll m
in section 41.1. (Finally, the physical
reason that the fields
X^{\rm adj}_{SU({\overline{N}}_{c})}
do not give by
themselves originating from the scale
\mu\sim\mu_{\rm x}
contributions to
particle masses is that they are too heavy now and so too short ranged
and  cannot   form the coherent condensate).

Clearly,
there are no any additional contributions to particle
masses in the Lagrangian (
41.4.3
),(
41.4.4
) at the scale

\mu=\mu_{\rm x}^{\rm pole}/({\rm several})
from
\langle X^{\rm adj}_{SU({\overline{N}}_{c})}\rangle_{\mu_{\rm x}^{\rm pole}/(\rm several)}=0
, see
(
41.4.19
), because all
{\overline{N}}_{c}^{\,2}-1
fields
X^{\rm adj}_{SU({\overline{N}}_{c})}
with masses
\mu_{\rm x}^{\rm pole}

decoupled already as
heavy
at
\mu=\mu_{\rm x}^{\rm pole}/(\rm several)
where (
41.4.19
) is
definitely valid, and they
do not affect by themselves the
dynamics of the lower energy
{\cal N}=1
theory at
\mu<\mu_{\rm x}^{\rm pole}/(\rm several)
. This is the reason for a qualitative difference   in
patterns of color symmetry breaking and mass spectra at
\mu_{\rm x}\ll m

in section 41.1 and at
\mu_{\rm x}\gg m
in this section. Remind that the
color breaking (and corresponding mass splitting) occurs at the scale

\mu\sim m\gg(\mu_{\rm x}m)^{1/2}\gg\mu_{\rm x}
and the unbroken gauge symmetry
looks as
SU({\overline{N}}_{c})\rightarrow SU({\rm n}_{1})\times U^{(2)}(1)\times SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})
in
section 41.1, while it occurs at the scale
m\ll\mu\sim(\mu_{\rm x}m)^{1/2}\ll\mu_{\rm x}
and the unbroken gauge symmetry looks as

SU({\overline{N}}_{c})\rightarrow SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})
in this section.

As it is seen from (
41.4.3
)-(
41.4.5
) and the whole
content of this section, the whole contributions
\sim(\mu_{\rm x}m)^{1/2}

to the masses of
SU({\rm n}_{1})
and hybrid gluons originate only at
lower energies
\mu\sim(\mu_{\rm x}m)^{1/2}\ll\mu_{\rm x}
in D-terms of the genuine
{\cal N}=1
SQCD
(
41.4.3
),(
41.4.4
) from higgsed quarks only. And the
whole additional F-term contributions
\sim m
to the quark masses
originate really from the quark self-interaction term
\sim{\rm Tr\,}(({\overline{Q}}Q))^{2}/\mu_{\rm x}
with higgsed quarks

\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{{\overline{N}}_{c}}=\langle{\overline{Q}}^{1}_{1}\rangle\langle Q^{1}_{1}\rangle
in (
41.4.3
) or, what is the same, from

\langle\Phi_{1,2}\rangle\sim m
in (
41.4.4
), this last also
originating finally from these higgsed quarks, forming the coherent
condensate and giving masses to corresponding gluons, see the last
line of (
41.4.5
). And the additional difference between the
section 41.1 with higgsed light
\langle\,X^{adj}_{SU({\overline{N}}_{c})}\rangle\sim\langle a_{2}\rangle\sim m
at
\mu_{\rm x}\ll m

in   (
41.1.3
),(
41.1.6
) contributing to quark masses, and
replacing it heavier
\langle\Phi^{j}_{i}\rangle\sim m
with the mass

\sim\mu_{\rm x}\gg m
in this
section, see (
41.4.4
),(
41.4.5
), is that
X^{adj}_{SU({\overline{N}}_{c})}
acts in the color space, while
\Phi^{j}_{i}
acts in
the flavor space.

42

Unbroken flavor symmetry,   S-vacua,
\mathbf{SU(N_{c})}

As in the br2 vacua in section 41.1, the non-trivial discrete

Z_{2N_{c}-N_{F}\geq 2}
symmetry is also unbroken in these vacua, i.e. they
also belong to the baryonic branch in the language
[
22
]
, this
case corresponds to
{\rm n}_{1}=0
. From (
40.3
),(
41.1.9
), the
quark condensates
\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)\rangle_{N_{c}}
in these vacua with the
multiplicity
{\overline{N}}_{c}
look as (neglecting smaller power corrections), see
section 4 in
[
17
]
,

\displaystyle\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)\rangle_{N_{c}}\approx-\,\frac{N_{c}}{{\overline{N}}_{c}}\,\mu_{\rm x}m=-\mu_{\rm x}\widetilde{m},\,\,\langle S\rangle_{N_{c}}=\Bigl(\frac{\det\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)\rangle_{N_{c}}}{\Lambda_{2}^{2N_{c}-N_{F}}\mu_{\rm x}^{N_{c}}}\Bigr)^{1/{\overline{N}}_{c}}\approx\mu_{\rm x}\widetilde{m}^{2}\Bigl(\frac{-\widetilde{m}}{\Lambda_{2}}\Bigr)^{(2N_{c}-N_{F})/{\overline{N}}_{c}}\ll\mu_{\rm x}m^{2},\,\,\,

(42.1)

\langle{\rm Tr\,}(\sqrt{2}\,X^{adj}_{SU(N_{c})})^{2}\rangle=\Bigl[(2N_{c}-N_{F})\langle S\rangle_{N_{c}}+m\langle{\rm Tr\,}({\overline{Q}}Q)\rangle_{N_{c}}\Bigr]\approx m\,(-N_{F}\mu_{\rm x}\widetilde{m})\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\widetilde{m}=\frac{N_{c}}{{\overline{N}}_{c}}m\,.

As before, the scalar
X^{\rm adj}_{SU(N_{c})}
higgses the
SU(N_{c})

group at the scale
\mu\sim\Lambda_{2}
as
SU(N_{c})\rightarrow SU({\overline{N}}_{c})\times U^{(1)}(1)\times U^{{\rm b}_{2}-1}(1)
, see (
41.1.1
),(
41.1.16
),

\displaystyle\langle X\rangle=\langle\,X^{adj}_{SU({\overline{N}}_{c})}+X^{(1)}_{U(1)}+X^{adj}_{{\rm b}_{2}}\,\rangle\,,\hskip 11.74988pt{\overline{N}}_{c}=N_{F}-N_{c}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt{\rm b}_{2}=2N_{c}-N_{F}\,,

(42.2)

\langle\sqrt{2}\,X^{adj}_{{\rm b}_{2}}\,\rangle=C_{{\rm b}_{2}}\Lambda_{2}\,{\rm diag}(\,\underbrace{\,0}_{{\overline{N}}_{c}}\,;\underbrace{\,\omega^{0},\,\,\omega^{1},\,...\,,\,\omega^{{\rm b}_{2}-1}}_{{\rm b}_{2}}\,)\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\omega=\exp\{\frac{2\pi i}{\,{\rm b}_{2}}\,\}\,,

\sqrt{2}\,X^{(1)}_{U(1)}=a_{1}\,{\rm diag}(\,\underbrace{\,1}_{{\overline{N}}_{c}}\,;\,\underbrace{\,c_{1}}_{{\rm b}_{2}}\,),\hskip 11.74988pt\,c_{1}=-\,\frac{{\overline{N}}_{c}}{{\rm b}_{2}}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\langle a_{1}\rangle=\frac{1}{c_{1}}m=\,-\frac{{\rm b}_{2}}{{\overline{N}}_{c}}m\,.

I.e., as in br2 vacua,
the same

{\rm b}_{2}=2N_{c}-N_{F}\geq 2
dyons

D_{j}
, massless in the limit
\mu_{\rm x}\rightarrow 0
(this number is required by
the unbroken
Z_{2N_{c}-N_{F}\geq 2}
discrete symmetry) are formed at
the   scale
\mu\sim\Lambda_{2}
and at sufficiently small
\mu_{\rm x}
the
superpotential
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}
at
m\ll\mu\ll\Lambda_{2}
is as in (
41.1.2
).

The difference is in the behavior of the
SU({\overline{N}}_{c})
part. In these
vacua with
{\rm n}_{1}=0
, there is no analog of the additional gauge
symmetry breaking
SU({\overline{N}}_{c})\rightarrow SU({\rm n}_{1})\times U^{(2)}(1)\times SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})
in br2 vacua at the scale
\mu\sim m
.

A)   At
\mu_{\rm x}\ll\Lambda^{SU({\overline{N}}_{c})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\ll m

and
N_{c}<N_{F}<2N_{c}-1
,
all quarks
in the
SU({\overline{N}}_{c})
part
in these S vacua have now
masses
\widetilde{m}=\langle m-a_{1}\rangle=mN_{c}/{\overline{N}}_{c}
and
decouple as heavy
in the weak coupling regime
at scales

\mu\,<\,\widetilde{m}/(\rm several)
, there remains
{\cal N}=2\,\,\,SU({\overline{N}}_{c})

SYM with the scale factor
\Lambda^{SU({\overline{N}}_{c})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}
of
its gauge coupling, see (
41.1.15
),

\displaystyle\langle\Lambda^{SU({\overline{N}}_{c})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle^{2{\overline{N}}_{c}}=\Bigl(\Lambda_{SU({\overline{N}}_{c})}=-\Lambda_{2}\Bigr)^{2{\overline{N}}_{c}-N_{F}}\,\widetilde{m}^{N_{F}}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\widetilde{m}=m\,\frac{N_{c}}{{\overline{N}}_{c}}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\langle\Lambda^{SU({\overline{N}}_{c})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle\ll\,m\,.

(42.3)

And the field
X^{adj}_{SU({\overline{N}}_{c})}
higgses this UV free

{\cal\,N}=2\,\,\,SU({\overline{N}}_{c})
SYM at the scale

\sim\langle\Lambda^{SU({\overline{N}}_{c})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle
in a
standard way,
SU({\overline{N}}_{c})\rightarrow U^{{\overline{N}}_{c}-1}(1)

[
31
]
. This results in the
right multiplicity
N_{s}={\overline{N}}_{c}=N_{F}-N_{c}
of these S vacua. The two single
roots with
(e^{+}-e^{-})\sim\Lambda^{SU({\overline{N}}_{c})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}

40.2
) originate here from this

SYM. Other

unequal double roots originating from this

SU({\overline{N}}_{c})
sector correspond to massless at
\mu_{\rm x}\rightarrow 0\,\,{\overline{N}}_{c}-1
pure
magnetic monopoles
M_{\rm n}
with the
SU({\overline{N}}_{c})
adjoint charges.

As a result of all described above, the low energy superpotential at

\mu_{\rm x}\ll(\langle\Lambda^{SU({\overline{N}}_{c})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle)^{2}/\Lambda_{2}

and at the scale

\mu<\langle\Lambda^{SU({\overline{N}}_{c})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle

can be written in these S-vacua as (the coefficients

in

) are known from
[
31
]
)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{W}}^{\,\rm low}_{\rm tot}={\mathcal{W}}^{\,(SYM)}_{SU({\overline{N}}_{c})}+{\mathcal{W}}^{\,\rm low}_{\rm matter}+\dots\,,\hskip 11.74988pt{\mathcal{W}}^{\,\rm low}_{\rm matter}={\mathcal{W}}_{D}+{\mathcal{W}}_{a_{1}}\,,

(42.4)

{\mathcal{W}}^{\,(SYM)}_{SU({\overline{N}}_{c})}={\overline{N}}_{c}\mu_{\rm x}(1+\delta_{2})\Bigl(\Lambda^{SU({\overline{N}}_{c})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\Bigr)^{2}+{\mathcal{W}}^{\,(M)}_{SU({\overline{N}}_{c})}\,,

{\mathcal{W}}^{\,(M)}_{SU({\overline{N}}_{c})}=-\sum_{n=1}^{{\overline{N}}_{c}-1}{\tilde{a}}_{M,\rm n}\Biggl[\,{\overline{M}}_{\rm n}M_{\rm n}+\mu_{\rm x}(1+\delta_{2})\Lambda^{SU({\overline{N}}_{c})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\Biggl(1+O\Bigl(\frac{\langle\Lambda^{SU({\overline{N}}_{c})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle}{m}\Bigr)\Biggr)f_{\rm n}\,\Biggr]\,,

{\mathcal{W}}_{D}=(m-c_{1}a_{1})\sum_{j=1}^{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{\overline{D}}_{j}D_{j}-\sum_{j=1}^{2N_{c}-N_{F}}a_{D,j}\,{\overline{D}}_{j}D_{j}\,-\,\mu_{\rm x}\Lambda_{2}\sum_{j=1}^{2N_{c}-N_{F}}\omega^{j-1}\,a_{D,j}+\mu_{\rm x}\,L_{S}\,\sum_{j=1}^{2N_{c}-N_{F}}a_{D,j}\,,

{\mathcal{W}}_{a_{1}}=\frac{\mu_{\rm x}}{2}(1+\delta_{1})\frac{{\overline{N}}_{c}N_{c}}{{\rm b}_{2}}a_{1}^{2}+\mu_{\rm x}N_{c}\delta_{3}a_{1}(m-c_{1}a_{1})+\mu_{\rm x}N_{c}\delta_{4}(m-c_{1}a_{1})^{2}\,,

where
\delta_{1,2}
are given in (
41.1.12
), while

, see (

).

From (
42.4
):

\displaystyle\langle a_{1}\rangle=\frac{1}{c_{1}}m=-\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{{\overline{N}}_{c}}\,m\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\langle a_{D,j}\rangle=\langle{\tilde{a}}_{M,\rm n}\rangle=0\,,

(42.5)

\langle{\overline{M}}_{\rm n}M_{\rm n}\rangle=\langle{\overline{M}}_{\rm n}\rangle\langle M_{\rm n}\rangle\approx\mu_{\rm x}\langle\Lambda^{SU({\overline{N}}_{c}}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle f_{n}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\,(2N_{c}-N_{F})\mu_{\rm x}\langle L_{S}\rangle=\langle\Sigma_{D}\rangle=\sum_{i=1}^{N_{F}}\langle{\overline{Q}}_{i}Q^{i}\rangle_{N_{c}}\,,

\langle{\overline{D}}_{j}D_{j}\rangle=\langle{\overline{D}}_{j}\rangle\langle D_{j}\rangle\approx\Bigl[\,-\mu_{\rm x}\Lambda_{2}\omega^{j-1}-\frac{N_{c}N_{F}}{{\overline{N}}_{c}(2N_{c}-N_{F})}\mu_{\rm x}m\,\Bigr]\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\Sigma_{D}=\sum_{j=1}^{2N_{c}-N_{F}}\langle{\overline{D}}_{j}\rangle\langle D_{j}\rangle\,.

And the qualitative situation with the condensates of
N_{F}
flavors of
heavy not higgsed quarks with

colors and masses

\sim\Lambda_{2}
in these S-vacua with

is the same as those in br2

)-(

),

\,\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)\rangle_{{\overline{N}}_{c}}=\langle S\rangle_{{\overline{N}}_{c}}/{\widetilde{m}}=-\mu_{\rm x}\langle\Lambda^{SU({\overline{N}}_{c})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle^{2}/{\widetilde{m}}\sim\mu_{\rm x}m\Bigl(m/\Lambda_{2}\Bigr)^{(2N_{c}-N_{F})/{\overline{N}}_{c}}\ll\mu_{\rm x}m\,
.

Besides, from (

),(

) (the leading terms only for

) )

\displaystyle\langle\Sigma_{D}\rangle=\langle{\rm Tr\,}({\overline{Q}}Q)\rangle_{N_{c}}\approx\langle{\rm Tr\,}({\overline{Q}}Q)\rangle_{2N_{c}-N_{F}}\approx-\frac{N_{c}\,N_{F}}{{\overline{N}}_{c}}\mu_{\rm x}m\,.

(42.6)

On the whole for the mass spectrum in these
{\overline{N}}_{c}
S-vacua with the
unbroken flavor symmetry at
\mu_{\rm x}\ll(\langle\Lambda^{SU({\overline{N}}_{c})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle)^{2}/\Lambda_{2}
. -

1) All original electric quarks
Q,{\overline{Q}}
are not higgsed but
confined. The masses of all original electric particles charged under

SU(2N_{c}-N_{F})
are the largest ones,
\sim\Lambda_{2}\,
, and they all are weakly
confined because all
2N_{c}-N_{F}
dyons
D_{j}
are higgsed,
\langle{\overline{D}}_{j}\rangle=\langle D_{j}\rangle\sim(\mu_{\rm x}\Lambda_{2})^{1/2}
, the string tension
in this sector is
\sigma^{1/2}_{SU(2N_{c}-N_{F})}\sim\langle D_{j}\rangle\sim(\mu_{\rm x}\Lambda_{2})^{1/2}\ll\Lambda_{2}\,
. These confined particles form a large number
of hadrons with masses
\sim\Lambda_{2}
.

2) The next mass scale is
\widetilde{m}
, these are masses of original electric
quarks with
SU({\overline{N}}_{c})
colors and
N_{F}
flavors, they are also weakly
confined due to higgsing of
{\overline{N}}_{c}-1
pure magnetic monopoles
M_{\rm n}
of
{\cal N}=2\,\,SU({\overline{N}}_{c})
SYM, the string tension here is much
smaller,
\sigma^{1/2}_{SU({\overline{N}}_{c})}\sim\langle M_{\rm n}\rangle_{SU({\overline{N}}_{c})}\sim(\mu_{\rm x}\langle\Lambda^{SU({\overline{N}}_{c})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle)^{1/2}\ll(\mu_{\rm x}\Lambda_{2})^{1/2}
. They form hadrons with masses
\sim m
.

3) The next mass scale is
\langle\Lambda^{SU({\overline{N}}_{c})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle\ll m
, see (
42.3
), these are masses of
charged
SU({\overline{N}}_{c})
electric gluons and scalars. They are also weakly confined
due to higgsing of
{\overline{N}}_{c}-1
magnetic monopoles
M_{\rm n}
, the tension
of the confining string is the same,
\sigma^{1/2}_{SU({\overline{N}}_{c})}\sim\langle M_{\rm n}\rangle_{SU({\overline{N}}_{c})}\sim(\mu_{\rm x}\langle\Lambda^{SU({\overline{N}}_{c})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle)^{1/2}
.
But the mass scale of these hadrons is

\sim\langle\Lambda^{SU({\overline{N}}_{c})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle\,
.

4) The other mass scale is
\sim(\mu_{\rm x}\Lambda_{2})^{1/2}
due to higgsing of

{\rm b}_{2}
dyons
D_{j}
. As a result,
{\rm b}_{2}=2N_{c}-N_{F}
long
{\cal N}=2

multiplets of massive photons with masses
\sim(\mu_{\rm x}\Lambda_{2})^{1/2}
are
formed (including
U^{(1)}(1)
with its scalar
a_{1}
).

5) The lightest are
{\overline{N}}_{c}-1
long
{\cal N}=2
multiplets of massive
dual photons with masses
\sim(\mu_{\rm x}\langle\Lambda^{SU({\overline{N}}_{c})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle)^{1/2}
.

6) Clearly, there are no massless Nambu-Goldstone particles because
the global flavor symmetry remains unbroken. And there are no massless
particles at all at
\mu_{\rm x}\neq 0,\,\,m\neq 0
.

The corresponding scalar multiplets have additional small
contributions
\sim\mu_{\rm x}
to their masses, these small corrections
break
{\cal N}=2
down to
{\cal N}=1
.

B)   At larger
\langle\Lambda^{SU({\overline{N}}_{c})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle\ll\mu_{\rm x}\ll\,m
, the difference is that all

SU({\overline{N}}_{c})
adjoint scalars
X^{adj}_{SU({\overline{N}}_{c})}
are now
too heavy
,
their physical
SU({\overline{N}}_{c})
phases induced by interactions with light

SU({\overline{N}}_{c})
gluons fluctuate freely at all scales
\mu\gtrsim\langle\Lambda^{SU({\overline{N}}_{c})}_{{\cal N}=1\,\,SYM}\rangle
, and
they are not higgsed. Instead, they decouple as heavy much before,
already at the scale
\mu<[\,\mu_{\rm x}^{\,\rm pole}=|g^{2}\mu_{\rm x}|\,]/{\rm several)}\ll m
, and there remains
{\cal N}=1\,\,SU({\overline{N}}_{c})
SYM
with the scale factor
\langle\Lambda^{SU({\overline{N}}_{c})}_{{\cal N}=1\,\,SYM}\rangle=[\,\widetilde{\mu}_{\rm x}\langle\Lambda^{SU({\overline{N}}_{c})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle^{2}\,]^{1/3}
of its gauge coupling,

\,\,\langle\Lambda^{SU({\overline{N}}_{c})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle\ll\langle\Lambda^{SU({\overline{N}}_{c})}_{{\cal N}=1\,\,SYM}\rangle\ll\mu_{\rm x}\ll\,m
. As in section 41.3, the small
nonzero value
\langle{\rm Tr\,}(X^{adj}_{SU({\overline{N}}_{c})})^{2}\rangle={\overline{N}}_{c}\langle S\rangle_{{\overline{N}}_{c}}/\widetilde{\mu}_{\rm x}={\overline{N}}_{c}\langle\Lambda^{SU({\overline{N}}_{c})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle^{2}\ll\langle\Lambda^{SU({\overline{N}}_{c})}_{{\cal N}=1\,\,SYM}\rangle^{2}
, see (
42.3
), arises here only due to the Konishi
anomaly, i.e. from one-loop Feynman diagrams with heavy scalars

X^{adj}_{SU({\overline{N}}_{c})}
and their fermionic superpartners with masses

\sim\mu_{\rm x}
inside, not because
X^{adj}_{SU({\overline{N}}_{c})}
are higgsed.

The multiplicity of vacua of this
{\cal N}=1\,\,SU({\overline{N}}_{c})
SYM is
also
{\overline{N}}_{c}
as it should be. There appears now a large number of
strongly coupled gluonia with the mass scale

\sim\langle\Lambda^{SU({\overline{N}}_{c})}_{{\cal N}=1\,\,SYM}\rangle
. All

SU({\overline{N}}_{c})
electrically charged particles are still confined, the
tension of the confining string is larger now,

\sigma^{1/2}_{SU({\overline{N}}_{c})}\sim\langle\Lambda^{SU({\overline{N}}_{c})}_{{\cal N}=1\,\,SYM}\rangle\ll\mu_{\rm x}\ll\,m
.

The case with
m\ll\mu_{\rm x}\ll\Lambda_{2}
is described in section 8.2 of

[
25
]
.

Part III.2.  Large quark
masses,
\Large\mathbf{m\gg\Lambda_{2}}

43

Broken flavor symmetry

We present below in this section the mass spectra at

m\gg\Lambda_{2}
of the direct (electric)
SU(N_{c})
theory (
40.1
) in
vacua with spontaneously broken flavor symmetry,
U(N_{F})\rightarrow U({\rm n}_{1})\times U({\rm n}_{2})
,
N_{c}+1<N_{F}<2N_{c}-1
. There are the br1-vacua
(br=breaking) with
1\leq{\rm n}_{1}<N_{F}/2\,,\,\,N_{F}={\rm n}_{1}+{\rm n}_{2}\,,
in which

\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{N_{c}}\gg\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{N_{c}}
, see
(
40.3
),(
40.4
). The quark and gluino
condensates look in these br1 vacua of
SU(N_{c})
as (the leading
terms   only),

\displaystyle\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{N_{c}}\approx\mu_{\rm x}m_{3}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{N_{c}}\approx\mu_{\rm x}m_{3}\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{2}}{m_{3}}\Bigr)^{\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}}\,,\hskip 11.74988ptm_{3}=\frac{N_{c}}{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}\,m\,,

(43.1)

\langle S\rangle_{N_{c}}=\frac{\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{N_{c}}\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{N_{c}}}{\mu_{\rm x}}\approx\mu_{\rm x}\,m_{3}^{2}\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{2}}{m_{3}}\Bigr)^{\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\frac{\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{N_{c}}}{\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{N_{c}}}\approx\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{2}}{m_{3}}\Bigr)^{\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}}\ll 1\,,

while
{\rm n}_{1}\leftrightarrow{\rm n}_{2}
in (
46.1.1
) in br2-vacua, but with

N_{F}/2<{\rm n}_{2}<N_{c}
in this case. The multiplicity of these br1 and br2
vacua is correspondingly
N_{\rm br1}=(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})C_{N_{F}}^{\,{\rm n}_{1}}

and
N_{\rm br2}=(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{2})C_{N_{F}}^{\,{\rm n}_{2}},\,\,C_{N_{F}}^{\,{\rm n}_{1}}=C_{N_{F}}^{\,{\rm n}_{2}}=[\,N_{F}!/{\rm n}_{1}!\,{\rm n}_{2}!\,],\,\,\,1\leq{\rm n}_{1}<N_{F}/2,\,\,{\rm n}_{2}>N_{F}/2
. It is seen from (
43.1
) that the
discrete
Z_{2N_{c}-N_{F}}
symmetry is unbroken at
m\gg\Lambda_{2}
in all these br1
and br2 vacua with
{\rm n}_{1}\neq{\overline{N}}_{c}
. In contrast,
Z_{2N_{c}-N_{F}}
symmetry is
(pure formally) broken spontaneously in special vacua with

{\rm n}_{1}={\overline{N}}_{c},\,\,{\rm n}_{2}=N_{c}
and the multiplicity
N_{spec}=(2N_{c}-N_{F})C_{N_{F}}^{\,{\overline{N}}_{c}}
(as it is really broken spontaneously in these
special vacua at
m\ll\Lambda_{2}
).

Out of them, see section 4 in
[
17
]
,
({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})C_{N_{F}}^{\,{\rm n}_{1}}

part of br1-vacua with

1\leq{\rm n}_{1}<{\overline{N}}_{c}
evolves at
m\ll\Lambda_{2}
into the br2-vacua of section
41.1   with
\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle^{(\rm br2)}_{N_{c}}\sim\mu_{\rm x}m\gg\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle^{(\rm br2)}_{N_{c}}
, while the other part of
br1-vacua and br2-vacua evolves at
m\ll\Lambda_{2}
into
Lt
vacua with

\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle^{(\rm Lt)}_{N_{c}}\sim\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle^{(\rm Lt)}_{N_{c}}\sim\mu_{\rm x}\Lambda_{2},\,,\,\langle S\rangle_{Lt}\sim\mu_{\rm x}\Lambda_{2}^{2}
.

43.1

SU(N_{c}),
br1 vacua, smaller

\mu_{\rm x},\,\,\,\mu_{\rm x}\ll\Lambda^{SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}

In the case considered,
X^{\rm adj}_{SU(N_{c})}
breaks the
SU(N_{c})

group
in the weak coupling regime
at the largest scale
\mu\sim m\gg\Lambda_{2}
as :
\,SU(N_{c})\rightarrow SU({\rm n}_{1})\times U(1)\times SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})
,
see (
43.1.4
) below,

\displaystyle\langle X^{\rm adj}_{SU(N_{c})}\rangle=\langle\,X^{adj}_{SU({\rm n}_{1})}+X_{U(1)}+X^{adj}_{SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}\,\rangle\,,

(43.1.1)

\sqrt{2}\,X_{U(1)}=a\,{\rm diag}\Bigl(\underbrace{\,1}_{{\rm n}_{1}}\,;\,\underbrace{\,c}_{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}\,\Bigr)\,,\hskip 11.74988ptc=-\frac{{\rm n}_{1}}{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}\,,\hskip 11.74988pta\equiv\langle a\rangle+{\hat{a}}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\langle a\rangle=m\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\langle{\hat{a}}\rangle\equiv 0\,.

As a result, all quarks
Q^{i}_{a},\,{\overline{Q}}_{j}^{\,a}
with flavors
i,j=1...N_{F}
and colors
a=({\rm n}_{1}+1)...N_{c}
have large masses

m_{3}=m-c\langle a\rangle=mN_{c}/(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})
, the hybrid gluons and
hybrid
X
also have masses
m_{3}\gg\Lambda_{2}
, and they all decouple at
scales
\mu\lesssim\,m_{3}
. The lower energy theory at
\mu<\,m_{3}

consists of
{\cal N}=2\,\,SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})
SYM, then
{\cal N}=2\,\,SU({\rm n}_{1})
SQCD with
N_{F}

with
{\rm n}_{1}
colors,

{\cal N}=2\,\,U(1)
photon
multiplet with its scalar superpartner
X_{U(1)}
. The scale factor of
the
{\cal N}=2\,\,\,SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})
SYM gauge coupling is

\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle\ll m
. The field
X^{\rm adj}_{SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}
in (
43.1.1
)) breaks
this
{\cal N}=2\,\,SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})
SYM at the scale

\mu\sim\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle
in a standard way,
SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})\rightarrow U(1)^{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}-1}

[
31
]

43.1
),

\displaystyle\langle\sqrt{2}\,X^{adj}_{SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}\rangle\sim\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle\,{\rm diag}\Bigl(\,\underbrace{\,0}_{{\rm n}_{1}}\,;k_{1},k_{2},\,...\,,k_{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}\Bigr)\,,\hskip 11.74988ptk_{i}=O(1)\,,

(43.1.2)

\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle^{2}\approx\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{2}^{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{(m_{3})}^{N_{F}}}{{(m_{3})}^{2{\rm n}_{1}}}\Bigr)^{\frac{1}{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}}\approx m^{2}_{3}\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{2}}{m_{3}}\Bigr)^{\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}}\ll m^{2}\,,

\langle S\rangle_{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}=\mu_{\rm x}\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal\,N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle^{2}=\langle S\rangle_{N_{c}}\,,

where
k_{i}
are known numbers
[
31
]
. We note that

\Lambda^{SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}
in (

) and

\langle a\rangle=m

) have the same charge 2 under
the discrete

transformations as
X
itself, this is due
to unbroken

symmetry. There are
N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}
physically

N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}-1
light pure magnetic monopoles
{\overline{M}}_{n},\,M_{n},\,\,n=1...N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}-1
with the

\mu_{\rm x}\rightarrow 0
) in each of these

SYM

\displaystyle{\cal W}^{\,\rm low}_{\rm tot}={\cal W}^{(M)}_{SYM}+{\cal W}_{{\rm n}_{1}}+\dots\,,

(43.1.3)

{\cal W}^{(M)}_{SYM}=-\sum_{n=1}^{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}-1}{\tilde{a}}_{M,\rm\,n}\Biggl[\,{\overline{M}}_{\rm n}M_{\rm\,n}+\mu_{\rm x}\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c}-{\rm\,n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle\Biggl(1+O\Bigl(\frac{\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal\,N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle}{m}\Bigr)\Biggr)z_{\rm n}\,\,\Biggr]\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\,\,z_{\rm n}=O(1),

where
z_{\rm n}
are known numbers
[
31
]
and dots denote
as always smaller power corrections.

All monopoles in (
43.1.3
) are higgsed at
\mu_{\rm x}\neq 0
with

\langle M_{\rm n}\rangle=\langle{\overline{M}}_{\rm n}\rangle\sim[\,\mu_{\rm x}\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle\,]^{1/2}
, so that all light particles of this

{\cal N}=2\,\,U(1)^{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}-1}
Abelian magnetic part form

N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}-1
long
{\cal N}=2
multiplets of massive dual photons
with masses
\sim(\mu_{\rm x}\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM})^{1/2}\rangle
.

Besides, for this reason, all heavier original
SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})

electrically charged particles with masses
\sim m
or

\sim\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle

are weakly confined (the confinement is weak in the sense that the
tension of the confining string is much smaller than their masses,

\sigma^{1/2}_{SYM}\sim(\mu_{\rm x}\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal\,N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle)^{1/2}\ll\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c}-{\rm\,n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle\ll m
).

And finally, as for the (independent)
{\cal N}=2\,\,SU({\rm n}_{1})\times U(1)
part with
N_{F}
flavors of original electric quarks
{\overline{Q}}^{b}_{j},Q^{\,i}_{b},\,\,i,j=1...N_{F},\,\,b=1...{\rm n}_{1}
. Its superpotential
at the low   scale looks as

\displaystyle{\cal W}_{{\rm n}_{1}}=(\,m-a\,)\,{\rm Tr}\,({\overline{Q}}Q)_{{\rm n}_{1}}-{\rm Tr}\,({\overline{Q}}\sqrt{2}X^{adj}_{SU({\rm n}_{1})}Q)+\mu_{\rm x}{\rm Tr}\,(X^{adj}_{SU({\rm n}_{1})})^{2}+\,

(43.1.4)

+\,\frac{\mu_{\rm x}}{2}\,\frac{{\rm n}_{1}N_{c}}{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}\,a^{2}+(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})\mu_{\rm x}\Biggl[\,\Bigl(\Lambda^{SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\Bigr)^{2}\approx\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{2}^{2N_{c}-N_{F}}(m_{3}-c\,{\hat{a}})^{N_{F}}}{[\,m_{3}+(1-c){\hat{a}}\,]^{2{\rm n}_{1}}}\Bigr)^{\frac{1}{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}}\,\Biggr]\,,\hskip 11.74988pta\equiv\langle a\rangle+{\hat{a}}\,.

Because the type of color breaking
SU(N_{c})\rightarrow SU({\rm n}_{1})\times U(1)\times SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})
in (
43.1.1
) is qualitatively different
from
SU(N_{c})\rightarrow SU(N_{F}-N_{c})\times U^{(1)}(1)\times U(1)^{2N_{c}-N_{F}-1}

in section 41.1 and occurs here
in the weak coupling regime

a(\mu\sim m)=N_{c}g^{2}(\mu\sim m)/8\pi^{2}\ll 1
, the nonperturbative
instanton contributions do not operate in this high energy region.
They originate and operate in these vacua only at much lower energies

\sim\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle

from the SYM part, see (
43.1.3
). For this reason, there are no
in (
43.1.4
) analogs of terms
\sim\delta_{i}
in (
41.1.2
).
From (
43.1.4
) (neglecting here small power corrections in the
quark condensate
\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{{\rm n}_{1}}
originating from the SYM part, see (
AW.4
) ), compare
also with (
43.1
),

\displaystyle\langle a\rangle=m\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\langle X^{adj}_{SU({\rm n}_{1})}\rangle=0\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\langle{\rm Tr}\,({\overline{Q}}Q)_{{\rm n}_{1}}\rangle={\rm n}_{1}\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{{\rm n}_{1}}+{\rm n}_{2}\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{{\rm n}_{1}}\approx{\rm n}_{1}\mu_{\rm x}\,m_{3}\,,

(43.1.5)

\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle^{SU(N_{c})}_{{\rm n}_{1}}=\langle{\overline{Q}}^{1}_{1}\rangle\langle Q^{1}_{1}\rangle\approx\mu_{\rm x}m_{3}\approx\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle^{SU(N_{c})}_{N_{c}}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{{\rm n}_{1}}=\sum_{a=1}^{{\rm n}_{1}}\langle{\overline{Q}}^{\,a}_{2}\rangle\langle Q^{2}_{a}\rangle=0\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\langle S\rangle_{{\rm n}_{1}}=0\,,

\langle Q^{i}_{b}\rangle=\langle{\overline{Q}}_{i}^{\,b}\rangle\approx\delta^{i}_{b}\,(\mu_{\rm x}m_{3})^{1/2},\,\,b=1...{\rm n}_{1},\,\,i=1...N_{F}\,,\,\,m_{3}=\frac{N_{c}}{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}\,.

On the whole, all this results in the right multiplicity of these br1
vacua,
N_{\rm br1}=(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})C_{N_{F}}^{\,{\rm n}_{1}}
, the factor
N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}

originates from
{\cal N}=2\,\,\,SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})
SYM, while

C_{N_{F}}^{\,{\rm n}_{1}}
is due to the spontaneous breaking
U(N_{F})\rightarrow U({\rm n}_{1})\times U({\rm n}_{2})
by
{\rm n}_{1}
higgsed quarks in the
SU({\rm n}_{1})\times U(1)
sector.

It is worth emphasizing that the value
\langle a\rangle=m
in
(
43.1.5
) is
exact
(i.e. there is no any small
correction). This is seen from the following. At very small
\mu_{\rm x}\rightarrow 0
, the possible correction
\delta m\neq 0
is independent of
\mu_{\rm x}
,
and so
\delta m\gg\mu_{\rm x}
. Then quarks
Q^{i}_{b},{\overline{Q}}_{i}^{\,b},\,\,i=1...N_{F},\,\,b=1...{\rm n}_{1}
will acquire masses
\delta m
, much larger
than the scale of their potentially possible coherent condensate,

\delta m\gg(\mu_{\rm x}m)^{1/2},\,\,\mu_{\rm x}\rightarrow 0
. In this case, these quarks
will be not higgsed but will decouple as heavy ones at scales

\mu<\delta m
and the flavor symmetry will remain unbroken.
There will remain
{\cal N}=2\,\,SU({\rm n}_{1})\times U(1)
SYM at scales

\mu<\delta m
. This lowest energy
{\cal N}=2\,\,SU({\rm n}_{1})
SYM
will give then its own multiplicity factor
{\rm n}_{1}
, so that the overall
multiplicity of vacua will be
{\rm n}_{1}(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})
, while the right
multiplicity is
(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})C^{\,{\rm n}_{1}}_{N_{F}}
, see (
43.1
).
To have this right multiplicity and spontaneous flavor symmetry
breaking all
{\rm n}_{1}
quarks have to be higgsed at arbitrary small

\mu_{\rm x}
,   and this is only possible when they are
exactly massless

in the limit
\mu_{\rm x}\rightarrow 0
, i.e. at
\delta m=0
.

57

57

These considerations clearly concern also all other
similar cases.

As a result of higgsing of
{\rm n}_{1}
out of
N_{F}
flavors of pure
electric original quarks in

SU({\rm n}_{1})\times U(1)
in (
43.1.4

, the

with flavors

complex degrees of freedom), while all other particles

) will acquire masses
\sim(\mu_{\rm x}m)^{1/2}
and will
form
{\rm n}_{1}^{2}
long
{\cal N}=2
multiplets of massive gluons.

On the whole, the hierarchies of nonzero masses look in this case as

\displaystyle\langle M_{\rm n}\rangle\sim(\mu_{\rm x}\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle)^{1/2}\ll\langle Q^{i}_{b=i}\rangle\sim(\mu_{\rm x}m)^{1/2}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\Lambda_{2}\ll\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle\ll m\,,

(43.1.6)

where
{\rm n}=1...N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}-1\,,\,\,\,i=1...{\rm n}_{1}
. There are no
massless particles, except for the
\,2{\rm n}_{1}{\rm n}_{2}
Nambu-Goldstone
multiplets.

The curve (
40.2
) has
N_{c}-1
double roots in these vacua.

N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}-1
unequal roots correspond to pure magnetic monopoles

M_{\rm n}
in (
43.1.3

{\rm n}_{1}
equal ones correspond to
higgsed quarks from
SU({\rm n}_{1})
. The two single roots with

(e^{+}-e^{-})\sim\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle
originate from SYM.

The calculation of leading power corrections to
\langle{\cal W}^{\,\rm low}_{\rm tot}\rangle
is presented in Appendix A,
and those to
\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle^{SU(N_{c})}_{{\rm n}_{1}}
are presented
in Appendices A and B. These latter are also compared therein
with the
independent

\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle^{SU(N_{c})}_{{\rm n}_{1}}
using   the roots of the curve (
