).

Now, let us recall some additional explanations about notations in
(
43.1.4
),(
43.1.5
). As for
higgsed

\langle a\rangle
, it looks as
\langle a\rangle=\langle a\rangle^{(\rm several)m}_{m/(\rm several)}=m
. In other words, its total mean
value   originates   and saturates at
\mu\sim m
. If not, all quarks will
decouple as heavy at
\mu=m/(\rm several)
, the multiplicity of these
br1 vacua will be wrong and
U(N_{F})
will remain unbroken. As for

\langle Q_{a}\rangle,\,\,a=1...{\rm n}_{1}
, of light quarks with
SU({\rm n}_{1})

colors. - At scales
[\mu^{\rm low}_{\rm cut}=(\mu_{\rm x}\,m_{3})^{1/2}]<\mu<m/(\rm several)
, all
SU({\rm n}_{1})
gluons are effectively   massless
and all light quarks with
SU({\rm n}_{1})
colors move freely and

independently
. Therefore, the physical (i.e. path dependent)

SU({\rm n}_{1})
phases of these quarks induced by their interactions with

SU({\rm n}_{1})
gluons fluctuate
freely
in this higher energy region,
so that

\displaystyle\langle{\overline{Q}}^{\,b}_{j}Q^{i}_{a}\rangle_{\mu^{\rm low}_{\rm cut}}=\langle{\overline{Q}}^{\,b}_{j}\rangle_{\mu^{\rm low}_{\rm cut}}\langle Q^{i}_{a}\rangle_{\mu^{\rm low}_{\rm cut}}=0\,,\hskip 11.74988pta,b=1...{\rm n}_{1}\,,\hskip 11.74988pti,j=1...N_{F}\,\hskip 11.74988pt\mu^{\rm low}_{\rm cut}=(\mu_{\rm x}\,m_{3})^{1/2}\,.

(43.1.7)

Therefore, in the whole region
0\leq\mu<m/(\rm several)
, the
nonzero quark mean values in (
43.1.5
), i.e.

[\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{{\rm n}_{1}}=\langle{\overline{Q}}^{1}_{1}\rangle\langle Q^{1}_{1}\rangle\,]/\mu_{\rm x}\approx[\langle a\rangle=m]N_{c}/(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})]
(the approximation
here is controllable and consists in neglecting small power
corrections originating from the SYM part, see
(
43.1.3
),(
43.1.4
) and Appendix A) originate and saturate

only
in the region
g(\mu_{\rm x}m_{3})^{1/2}/(\rm several)<\mu<(\rm several){\it g}(\mu_{\rm x}\,m_{3})^{1/2}
, after
{\rm n}_{1}
out of
N_{F}
quarks
are higgsed (i.e. form the coherent condensate),
giving masses

g(\mu_{\rm x}m)^{1/2}
to themselves and all
SU({\rm n}_{1})\times U(1)

gluons.   And there remain only
2{\rm n}_{1}{\rm n}_{1}
massless Nambu-
Goldstone multiplets in this sector at lower energies.

And there is no contradiction in the relation following from
(
43.1.4
)

\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{{\rm n}_{1}}=\Bigl(\langle{\overline{Q}}^{1}_{1}\rangle\langle Q^{1}_{1}\rangle\Bigr)^{(\rm several){\it g}_{Q}(\mu_{\rm x}m_{3})^{1/2}}_{g_{Q}(m_{3}\mu_{\rm x})^{1/2}/(\rm several)}\approx\Bigl[\mu_{\rm x}\frac{N_{c}}{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}\Bigl(\langle a\rangle^{(\rm several)m}_{m/(\rm several)}=m\,\Bigr)=\mu_{\rm x}m_{3}\,\Bigr],

in that the
total mean values
of
\langle a\rangle
and

\langle Q^{1}_{1}\rangle
originate and saturate in different energy regions,
because this is
not
the equality between mean values of two
equal to each other operators, but only the numerical equality between
two total mean values of two different operators.

On the whole, the total decomposition of quark condensates
\langle(({\overline{Q}}Q))_{1,2}\rangle_{N_{c}}
over their separate color parts look in
these br1 vacua as follows.

I) The condensate
\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{N_{c}}
.

a) From (
43.1.5
) and accounting for the leading power
correction, see (
AW.5
), the (factorizable) condensate of
higgsed quarks in the
SU({\rm n}_{1})
part

\displaystyle\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{{\rm n}_{1}}\approx\mu_{\rm x}\,m_{3}\Biggl[1-\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{2}}{m_{3}}\Bigr)^{\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}}\Biggr],\,\,m_{3}=\frac{N_{c}}{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}\,m\gg\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle\gg\Lambda_{2}.\,\,\,\,\,

(43.1.8)

b) The (non-factorizable) condensate
\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}
is
determined by the one-loop Konishi anomaly for the heavy not higgsed
quarks with the mass
m_{3}
in the
SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})
SYM sector. I.e., on
the first ”preliminary” stage, from the one-loop diagrams with heavy
scalar quarks and their fermionic superpartners inside, transforming
in the weak coupling regime at the scale
\mu\sim m
the heavy quark

operator

(({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1})_{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}
into the
operator

\sim(\lambda\lambda)_{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}/m_{3}
of lighter
SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})
gluino. And
the mean value of this latter originates only at much lower energies

\mu\lesssim\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle
, see (
41.1.2
),

\displaystyle\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}=\frac{\langle S\rangle_{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}}{m_{3}}=\frac{\mu_{\rm x}\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle^{2}}{m_{3}}\approx\mu_{\rm x}m_{3}\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{2}}{m_{3}}\Bigr)^{\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}}\,.

(43.1.9)

Therefore, on the whole

\displaystyle\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{N_{c}}=\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{{\rm n}_{1}}+\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}\approx\,\mu_{\rm x}m_{3}\Biggl[1+\frac{{\rm n}_{2}-N_{c}}{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{2}}{m_{3}}\Bigr)^{\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{{\rm n}_{2}-N_{c}}}\Biggr]\,,\,\,\hskip 11.74988pt

(43.1.10)

as it should be, see (
AW.1
).

II) The condensate
\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{N_{c}}
.

a) From
(
43.1.5
), the (factorizable) condensate of non-higgsed
massless Nambu-Goldstone particles in the
SU({\rm n}_{1})
part

\displaystyle\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{{\rm n}_{1}}=\sum_{a=1}^{{\rm n}_{1}}\langle{\overline{Q}}^{\,a}_{2}\rangle\langle Q^{2}_{a}\rangle=0\,.

(43.1.11)

b) The (non-factorizable) condensate
\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}
is
determined by the same one-loop Konishi anomaly for the heavy
non-higgsed quarks with the mass
m_{3}
in the
SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})
SYM
sector,

\displaystyle\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}=\frac{\langle S\rangle_{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}}{m_{3}}\approx\mu_{\rm x}m_{3}\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{2}}{m_{3}}\Bigr)^{\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}}\,.

(43.1.12)

Therefore, on the whole

\displaystyle\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{N_{c}}=\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{{\rm n}_{1}}+\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}\approx\mu_{\rm x}\,m_{3}\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{2}}{m_{3}}\Bigr)^{\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}}\,,

(43.1.13)

as it should be, see (
AW.1
).

43.2

U(N_{c})
, br1 vacua, smaller
\mu_{\rm x},\,\,\mu_{\rm x}\ll\Lambda^{SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}

When the additional
U^{(0)}(1)
is introduced, the Konishi anomalies
look as in (
41.2.2
) but
\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{N_{c}}
is dominant
in br1 vacua, i.e. (the leading terms
only)

\displaystyle\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}+({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{N_{c}}=\mu_{\rm x}m\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{N_{c}}=\mu_{\rm x}m-\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{N_{c}}\approx\mu_{\rm x}m\,,

(43.2.1)

\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{N_{c}}\approx\mu_{\rm x}m\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{2}}{m}\Bigr)^{\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}}\approx\mu_{\rm x}m\,\frac{\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle^{2}}{m^{2}}\,,

while

\displaystyle\frac{\langle a_{0}\rangle}{m}=\frac{1}{\mu_{\rm x}m\,N_{c}}\langle{\rm Tr}\,({\overline{Q}}Q)\rangle_{N_{c}}\approx\Bigl[\,\frac{{\rm n}_{1}}{N_{c}}+\frac{{\rm n}_{2}-{\rm n}_{1}}{N_{c}}\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{2}}{m}\Bigr)^{\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}}\,\Bigr]\approx\frac{{\rm n}_{1}}{N_{c}}\,.

(43.2.2)

(Put attention that
\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle
in this section is different from those in
section 43.1, see (
43.1.2
)).

Instead of (
43.1.4
) the superpotential of the
SU({\rm n}_{1})\times U^{(0)}(1)\times U(1)
part looks now as

{\cal W}^{\,\rm low}_{\rm matter}={\mathcal{W}}_{{\rm n}_{1}}+{\mathcal{W}}_{a_{0},a}+\dots\,,

\displaystyle{\cal W}_{{\rm n}_{1}}=(m-a_{0}-a){\rm Tr}({\overline{Q}}Q)_{{\rm n}_{1}}-{\rm Tr}({\overline{Q}}\sqrt{2}X^{adj}_{SU({\rm n}_{1})}Q)+\mu_{\rm x}{\rm Tr}(X^{adj}_{SU({\rm n}_{1})})^{2},\,\,\,

(43.2.3)

{\mathcal{W}}_{a_{0},a}=\frac{\mu_{\rm x}}{2}N_{c}a^{2}_{0}+\frac{\mu_{\rm x}}{2}\frac{{\rm n}_{1}N_{c}}{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}a^{2}\,,

where dots denote smaller power corrections.

From (
43.2.3
) (for the leading terms)

\langle a_{0}\rangle\approx\frac{{\rm n}_{1}}{N_{c}}m\,,\,\,\,\langle a\rangle=\langle m-a_{0}\rangle\approx\frac{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}{N_{c}}m\,,\,\,\,\langle{\rm Tr}\,({\overline{Q}}Q)\rangle_{{\rm n}_{1}}\approx\mu_{\rm x}N_{c}\langle a_{0}\rangle\approx\mu_{\rm x}\frac{{\rm n}_{1}N_{c}}{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}\langle a\rangle\approx{\rm n}_{1}\mu_{\rm x}\,m\,,

\displaystyle\langle X^{adj}_{SU({\rm n}_{1})}\rangle=0,\,\,\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle^{U(N_{c})}_{{\rm n}_{1}}=\sum_{a=1}^{{\rm n}_{1}}\langle{\overline{Q}}^{\,a}_{1}Q^{1}_{a}\rangle=\langle{\overline{Q}}^{\,a}_{1}\rangle\langle Q^{1}_{a}\rangle\approx\mu_{\rm x}m\approx\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle^{U(N_{c})}_{N_{c}},\,\,\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{{\rm n}_{1}}=0.\hskip 11.74988pt\,\,\,

(43.2.4)

The qualitative difference with section 4.1 is that one extra
{\cal N}=1
photon multiplet remains massless now in this
U(N_{c})
theory
while corresponding scalar multiplet has the smallest mass
\sim\mu_{\rm x}

because
{\cal N}=2
is broken down to
{\cal N}=1
at the level

O(\mu_{\rm x})
.

The calculation of leading power corrections to
\langle{\cal W}^{\,\rm low}_{\rm tot}\rangle
in these
U(N_{c})
br1 vacua is
presented in Appendix A, and those to

\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle^{U(N_{c})}_{{\rm n}_{1}}
are presented in Appendices A and
B. These latter are also compared therein with the
independent

calculations of
\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle^{U(N_{c})}_{{\rm n}_{1}}
using the roots of
the curve (
40.2
).

43.3

SU(N_{c})
, br1 vacua, larger
\mu_{\rm x},\,\Lambda^{SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\ll\mu_{\rm x}\ll m

Nothing changes significantly in this case with the
SU({\rm n}_{1})\times U(1)
part in (
43.1.4
). The mean value
\langle a\rangle=m\gg\mu_{\rm x}
of the field
\langle\sqrt{2}\,X_{U(1)}\rangle
in
(
43.1.4
) stays intact as far as
\mu_{\rm x}\ll m
, and so the
contributions
\sim(\mu_{\rm x}m)^{1/2}
to the masses of
{\rm n}_{1}^{2}-1
long

{\cal N}=2\,\,\,SU({\rm n}_{1})
multiplets of massive gluons and to the mass   of
the long
{\cal N}=2\,\,\,U(1)
multiplet of the massive photon due   to
higgsing of
{\rm n}_{1}
quarks remain dominant. At this level the
{\cal N}=2
SUSY remains unbroken in this sector. It breaks down here to

{\cal N}=1
only at the level
\sim\mu_{\rm x}
by the additional smaller
contributions
\sim\mu_{\rm x}\ll(\mu_{\rm x}m)^{1/2}
to the masses of
corresponding scalar multiplets.

The situation with
SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})
SYM in
(
43.1.2
),(
43.1.3
) is different. At
\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle\ll\mu_{\rm x}\ll m
, the adjoint
scalars
X^{adj}_{SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}
of the
SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})
subgroup in
(
43.1.2
) become too heavy and too short ranged. Their light

SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})
physical (i.e. path dependent) phases induced by
interactions with effectively massless at all scales

\mu\gtrsim\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=1\,\,SYM}\rangle\,\,SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})
gluons fluctuate freely at
the scale
\mu>\mu_{\rm x}^{\rm pole}/(\rm several)={\it g}^{2}\mu_{\rm x}/(\rm several)
, and they are

not   higgsed
, i.e.
\langle X^{adj}_{SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}\rangle_{\mu_{\rm x}^{\rm pole}/(\rm several)}=0
. Instead, they decouple as heavy already at
the scale
\mu<\mu_{\rm x}^{\rm pole}/(\rm several)\ll m
in the weak coupling
region, can all be integrated out and do not affect by themselves the
lower energy dynamics in this
SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})
sector. There remains

{\cal N}=1\,\,SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})
SYM with the scale factor

\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=1\,\,SYM}\rangle=[\,\mu_{\rm x}\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle^{2}\,]^{1/3}

of   its gauge coupling,
\,\,\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle\ll\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=1\,\,SYM}\rangle\ll\mu_{\rm x}\ll\,m
,

\displaystyle\langle S\rangle_{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}=\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=1\,\,SYM}\rangle^{3}=\mu_{\rm x}m_{3}^{2}\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{2}}{m_{3}}\Bigr)^{\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}},\hskip 11.74988pt\frac{\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=1\,\,SYM}\rangle}{\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle}\sim\Biggl(\frac{\mu_{\rm x}}{\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle}\Biggr)^{1/3}\gg 1\,.

(43.3.1)

The small (non-factorizable) value
\langle{\rm Tr\,}(X^{adj}_{SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})})^{2}\rangle=(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})\langle S\rangle_{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}/\mu_{\rm x}=(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle^{2}\\
\ll\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=1\,\,SYM}\rangle^{2}
arises here not because

X^{adj}_{SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}
are higgsed, but only due to the Konishi
anomaly. I.e. from one-loop diagrams with heavy scalars

X^{adj}_{SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}
and their fermionic superpartners with masses

\sim\mu_{\rm x}
inside. On the first ”preliminary” stage these loop effects
transform, in the weak coupling regime at the scale
\sim\mu_{\rm x}
, the
heavy field
operator

{\rm Tr\,}(X^{adj}_{SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})})^{2}
into   the

operator

\sim{\rm Tr\,}(\lambda\lambda)_{SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}/\mu_{\rm x}
of lighter

{\cal\,N}=1\,\,SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})
SYM gluinos. And the nonzero
mean value of this latter originates and saturates only in the strong
coupling and non-perturbative regime at much lower energies

\sim\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=1\,\,SYM}\rangle\ll\mu_{\rm x}
.
There is now a large number of strongly coupled gluonia with the mass
scale
\sim\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=1\,\,SYM}\rangle
in this
{\cal N}=1
SYM.
The multiplicity of vacua in this sector remains equal
N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}
as it
should be.   All heavier original electric particles with masses
\sim m
or
\sim\mu_{\rm x}
charged with respect to
SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})
remain weakly
confined, but the string tension is larger now,
\sigma^{1/2}_{{\cal N}=2}\ll\sigma^{1/2}_{{\cal N}=1}\sim\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=1\,\,SYM}\rangle\ll\mu_{\rm x}\ll m
.

43.4

SU(N_{c})
,   special vacua

The values of quark condensates in these special vacua with

{\rm n}_{1}={\overline{N}}_{c},\,{\rm n}_{2}=N_{c}
, see
[
25
]
and (
43.1
),

\displaystyle\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}+({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}-\frac{1}{N_{c}}{\rm Tr}\,(({\overline{q}}q))\rangle_{N_{c}}=(1-\frac{{\rm n}_{1}={\overline{N}}_{c}}{N_{c}})\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{N_{c}}=\mu_{\rm x}\,m\,,

(43.4.1)

\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{N_{c}}=\frac{N_{c}}{2N_{c}-N_{F}}\mu_{\rm x}m=\mu_{\rm x}m_{3}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{N_{c}}=\mu_{\rm x}\Lambda_{2}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\langle S\rangle_{N_{c}}=\frac{\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{N_{c}}\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{N_{c}}}{\mu_{\rm x}}=

=\Bigl(\frac{\det\langle({\overline{q}}q)\rangle_{N_{c}}}{\Lambda_{2}^{2N_{c}-N_{F}}\mu_{\rm x}^{N_{c}}}\Bigr)^{1/{\overline{N}}_{c}}=\langle S\rangle_{2N_{c}-N_{F}}=\mu_{\rm x}\langle\Lambda^{SU(2N_{c}-N_{F})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle^{2}=\mu_{\rm x}m_{3}\Lambda_{2}\,,

are exact and valid at any values
m\gtrless\Lambda_{2}
. Therefore, the
discrete
Z_{2N_{c}-N_{F}}
symmetry is formally broken spontaneously therein
at large
m\gg\Lambda_{2}
also, as it is really broken at
m\ll\Lambda_{2}
. But
practically they behave at
m\gg\Lambda_{2}
as the br1 vacua described above
in section (43.1) with
{\rm n}_{1}={\overline{N}}_{c}\,
. I.e., the factor
2N_{c}-N_{F}
in their
multiplicity
N_{\rm spec}=(2N_{c}-N_{F})C^{\,{\overline{N}}_{c}}_{N_{F}}
originates at

m\gg\Lambda_{2}
from the multiplicity
2N_{c}-N_{F}
of the
SU(2N_{c}-N_{F})\,\,{\cal N}=2

SYM at
\mu_{\rm x}\ll\langle\Lambda^{SU(2N_{c}-N_{F})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle

or
{\cal N}=1
SYM at
\langle\Lambda^{SU(2N_{c}-N_{F})}_{{\cal N}=1\,\,SYM}\rangle\ll\mu_{\rm x}\ll m
, after the breaking
SU(N_{c})\rightarrow SU({\overline{N}}_{c})\times U(1)\times SU(2N_{c}-N_{F})
by
\langle X_{U(1)}\rangle
at
the scale
\mu=m_{3}\gg\Lambda_{2}
, see (
43.4.1
). The factor

C^{\,{\overline{N}}_{c}}_{N_{F}}
originates due to higgsing of
{\rm n}_{1}={\overline{N}}_{c}
original
electric quarks in the
SU({\overline{N}}_{c})
sector,

\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{{\overline{N}}_{c}}=\langle{\overline{Q}}^{\,1}_{1}\rangle\langle Q^{1}_{1}\rangle=\mu_{\rm x}\,m_{3}
, see (
43.4.1
), and
spontaneous breaking
U(N_{F})\rightarrow U({\overline{N}}_{c})\times U(N_{c})
. The two single
roots with
(e^{+}-e^{-})\sim\langle\Lambda^{SU(2N_{c}-N_{F})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle
of the curve (
40.2
) at small
\mu_{\rm x}

originate from the
{\cal N}=2\,\,SU(2N_{c}-N_{F})
SYM. There are no massless
particles at
\mu_{\rm x}\neq 0
, except for the standard
2{\rm n}_{1}{\rm n}_{2}

Nambu-Goldstone multiplets for equal mass quarks.

44

Unbroken flavor symmetry,   SYM vacua,
\mathbf{SU(N_{c})}

These SYM vacua of the
SU(N_{c})
theory have the multiplicity
N_{c}
.

\langle X^{\rm adj}\rangle_{N_{c}}\sim\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c})}_{{\cal N}=2\,SYM}\rangle\ll m
in these
vacua, so that all quarks have here large masses

m\gg\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c})}_{{\cal N}=2\,SYM}\rangle
and
decouple in the weak coupling regime
at
\mu<m/(\rm several)
. There remains at this scale the
{\cal N}=2\,\,SU(N_{c})
SYM with the scale factor of its gauge coupling:

\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c})}_{{\cal N}=2\,SYM}\rangle^{2}=(\Lambda_{2}^{2N_{c}-N_{F}}m^{N_{F}})^{1/N_{c}}=m^{2}\,(\Lambda_{2}/m)^{(2N_{c}-N_{F})/N_{c}},\,\,\,\Lambda_{2}\ll\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c})}_{{\cal N}=2\,SYM}\rangle\ll m
.

If
\mu_{\rm x}\ll\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c})}_{{\cal N}=2\,SYM}\rangle
, then
the field
X^{\rm adj}_{SU(N_{c})}
is higgsed at the scale

\mu\sim\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c})}_{{\cal N}=2\,SYM}\rangle
in a
standard way for the
{\cal N}=2
SYM,
\langle\sqrt{2}X^{\rm adj}_{SU(N_{c})}\rangle\sim\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c})}_{{\cal N}=2\,SYM}\rangle\,{\rm diag}\,(k_{1},k_{2},\,...\,k_{N_{c}}),\,\,k_{i}=O(1)

[
31
]
,
SU(N_{c})\rightarrow U^{N_{c}-1}(1)
. Note that the value

\sim\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c})}_{{\cal N}=2\,SYM}\rangle
of
\langle X^{\rm adj}_{SU(N_{c})}\rangle
is consistent with the unbroken

Z_{2N_{c}-N_{F}}
discrete symmetry.

All original electrically charged gluons and scalars acquire masses

\sim\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c})}_{{\cal N}=2\,SYM}\rangle
and
N_{c}-1

lighter Abelian pure magnetic monopoles
M_{k}
are formed. These are
higgsed at
\mu_{\rm x}\neq 0
and
N_{c}-1
long
{\cal N}=2
multiplets of
massive dual photons are formed, with masses
\sim(\mu_{\rm x}\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c})}_{{\cal N}=2\,SYM}\rangle)^{1/2}\ll\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c})}_{{\cal N}=2\,SYM}\rangle
(there are non-leading contributions
\sim\mu_{\rm x}\ll(\mu_{\rm x}\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c})}_{{\cal N}=2\,SYM}\rangle)^{1/2}
to the
masses of corresponding scalars, these break slightly
{\cal N}=2

down to
{\cal N}=1
). As a result, all original electrically charged
quarks, gluons and scalars
X
are weakly confined (i.e. the tension
of the confining string,
\sigma^{1/2}_{2}\sim(\mu_{\rm x}\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c})}_{{\cal N}=2\,SYM}\rangle)^{1/2}
, is
much smaller than their masses
\sim m
or

\sim\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c})}_{{\cal N}=2\,SYM}\rangle\,
).

All
N_{c}-1
double roots of the curve (
40.2
) correspond in this
case to
N_{c}-1
pure magnetic monopoles (massless at
\mu_{\rm x}\rightarrow 0
),
while two single roots with
(e^{+}-e^{-})\sim\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c})}_{{\cal N}=2\,SYM}\rangle
also
originate from this
{\cal N}=2\,\,SU(N_{c})
SYM.

The mass spectrum is different if
\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c})}_{{\cal N}=2\,SYM}\ll\mu_{\rm x}\ll m
. All scalar fields
X^{\rm adj}_{SU(N_{c})}
are
then too heavy and not higgsed. Instead, they decouple as heavy at
scales
\mu<\mu_{\rm x}^{\rm pole}/(\rm several)
, still in the weak coupling
regime, and there remains at lower energies
{\cal N}=1\,\,SU(N_{c})

SYM with its
N_{c}
vacua and with the scale factor

\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c})}_{{\cal N}=1\,SYM}\rangle
of its gauge
coupling,
\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c})}_{{\cal N}=1\,SYM}\rangle=[\,\mu_{\rm x}(\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c})}_{{\cal N}=2\,SYM}\rangle)^{\,2}\,]^{1/3},\,\,\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c})}_{{\cal N}=2\,SYM}\rangle\ll\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c})}_{{\cal N}=1\,SYM}\rangle\ll\mu_{\rm x}\ll m
. A large number of strongly coupled gluonia
with the mass scale
\sim\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c})}_{{\cal N}=1\,SYM}\rangle
is formed in this
{\cal N}=1
SYM theory, while all
heavier original charged particles are weakly confined (the tension of
the confining string,

\sigma^{1/2}_{1}\sim\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c})}_{{\cal N}=1\,SYM}\rangle
, is much smaller than the quark masses
\sim m
or the
scalar masses
\sim\mu_{\rm x}
).

45

Very special vacua with
\mathbf{\langle S\rangle_{N_{c}}=0}
in

\mathbf{U(N_{c})}
gauge theory

We consider in this section the vs (very special) vacua
with
{\rm n}_{1}={\overline{N}}_{c},\,{\rm n}_{2}=N_{c},\,N_{c}<N_{F}<2N_{c}-1
,
\langle S\rangle_{N_{c}}=0
( as always,
\langle S\rangle_{N_{c}}
is the
bilinear gluino condensate summed over all its colors) and the
multiplicity
N_{vs}=C^{{\overline{N}}_{c}}_{N_{F}}=C^{N_{c}}_{N_{F}}
in the

U(N_{c})=SU(N_{c})\times U^{(0)}(1)
theory, when the additional Abelian

U^{(0)}(1)
with
\mu_{0}=\mu_{\rm x}
is added to the
SU(N_{c})
theory (see
e.g.
[
41
]
and references therein, these are called
r=N_{c}
vacua
in
[
41
]
). Note that vacua with
\langle S\rangle_{N_{c}}=0

\displaystyle{\cal W}_{\rm matter}=\mu_{\rm x}\Bigl[{\rm Tr}\,(X_{0})^{2}=\frac{1}{2}N_{c}a_{0}^{2}\Bigr]+\mu_{\rm x}{\rm Tr}\,(X^{\rm adj}_{SU(N_{c})})^{2}+{\rm Tr}\,\Biggl[\,(m-a_{0})\,{\overline{Q}}Q-{\overline{Q}}\sqrt{2}X^{adj}_{SU(N_{c})}Q\Biggr]_{N_{c}}\,,

(45.1)

\sqrt{2}X_{0}=a_{0}\,{\rm diag}(\,\underbrace{\,1}_{N_{c}}\,)\,,\hskip 11.74988ptX^{\rm adj}_{SU(N_{c})}=X^{A}T^{A}\,,\,\,{\rm Tr}\,(T^{A}T^{B})=\frac{1}{2}\,\delta^{AB}\,,\hskip 11.74988ptA,B=1,\,...,\,N_{c}^{2}-1\,.

From (
45.1
) and Konishi anomalies

\displaystyle\langle a_{0}\rangle=\frac{\langle{\rm Tr}({\overline{Q}}\,Q)\rangle_{N_{c}}={\overline{N}}_{c}\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{N_{c}}+N_{c}\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{N_{c}}}{\mu_{\rm x}N_{c}}\,,

(45.2)

\langle m-a_{0}\rangle\sum_{a=1}^{N_{c}}\langle{\overline{Q}}_{i}^{a}Q^{i}_{a}\rangle-\sum_{A=1}^{N^{2}_{c}-1}\sum_{a,b=1}^{N_{c}}\langle{\overline{Q}}_{i}^{b}\sqrt{2}X^{A}(T^{A})^{a}_{b}Q^{i}_{a}\rangle=\langle S\rangle_{N_{c}},\,\,i=1...N_{F},\,\,\rm{no\,\,summation\,\,over\,\,flavor},

\mu_{\rm x}\langle{\rm Tr}\,(\sqrt{2}X^{\rm adj}_{SU(N_{c})})^{2}\rangle=(2N_{c}-N_{F})\langle S\rangle_{N_{c}}+\langle m-a_{0}\rangle\langle{\rm Tr}\,(({\overline{q}}q))\rangle_{N_{c}}\,.

45.1

m\gg\Lambda_{2}\,,\,\,\mu_{\rm x}\gg\Lambda_{2}^{2}/m\,,\,\,N_{c}<N_{F}<2N_{c}-1

Consider first this simplest case with

”eats” all quark masses

\displaystyle\langle{\overline{Q}}_{i=b+{\overline{N}}_{c}}^{\,b}\rangle=\langle Q^{i=b+{\overline{N}}_{c}}_{b}\rangle=(\mu_{\rm x}m)^{1/2}\gg\Lambda_{2}\,,\hskip 11.74988ptb=1...N_{c}\,,\hskip 11.74988pti={\overline{N}}_{c}+1...N_{F}\,,

(45.1.1)

\langle X^{adj,A}_{SU(N_{c})}\rangle={\sqrt{2}}\,{\rm Tr\,}\Bigl[\langle{\overline{Q}}\rangle T^{A}\langle Q\rangle\Bigr]/\mu_{\rm x}=0\,,\hskip 11.74988ptA=1...N_{c}^{2}-1\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\langle a_{0}\rangle=m\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\langle S\rangle_{N_{c}}=0\,.

N_{c}

\sim(\mu_{\rm x}m)^{1/2}\gg\Lambda_{2}

45.2

m\gg\Lambda_{2}\,,\,\,\mu_{\rm x}\ll\Lambda_{2}^{2}/m\,,\,\,N_{c}<N_{F}<2N_{c}-1

Consider now the case of smaller
\mu_{\rm x}
, such that
(\mu_{\rm x}m)^{1/2}\ll\Lambda_{2}
, while

remains large,
m\gg\Lambda_{2}
. The quark

\,\langle a_{0}\rangle=m

stays intact
and still ”eats” all quark masses

"m"
in (

). Therefore, because the
SU(N_{c})
theory is UV
free, its gauge coupling grows logarithmically with diminished energy
and, if nothing prevents, it will become
g^{2}(\mu)<0
at
\mu<\Lambda_{2}
.
Clearly, even if quarks were higgsed as above in section 45.1, i.e.

\langle Q^{2}\rangle=\langle{\overline{Q}}_{2}\rangle\sim(\mu_{\rm x}m)^{1/2}

(while
\langle X^{adj}_{SU(N_{c})}\rangle\ll\Lambda_{2}
), see (

), this would result only in appearance of

\sim(\mu_{\rm x}m)^{1/2}\ll\Lambda_{2}
, so that
all
particles would remain effectively massless at the scale

\mu\sim\Lambda_{2}\gg(\mu_{\rm x}m)^{1/2}
(this is especially clear in the
unbroken
{\cal N}=2
theory at
\mu_{\rm x}\rightarrow 0
). Therefore, this will not
help and the problem with
g^{2}(\mu<\Lambda_{2})<0
cannot be solved in this
way (these quarks
{\overline{Q}}_{2},Q^{2}
are really not higgsed now at all,
i.e.
\langle Q^{i}_{b}\rangle=\langle{\overline{Q}}_{i}^{\,b}\rangle=0,\,i={\overline{N}}_{c}+1...N_{F},\,b=1...N_{c}
, see below).

As explained in Introduction, to really avoid the unphysical

g^{2}(\mu<\Lambda_{2})<0
, the field
X^{adj}_{SU(N_{c})}
is higgsed
necessarily   in this case at
\mu\sim\Lambda_{2}
. Because the non-trivial

Z_{2N_{c}-N_{F}\geq 2}

discrete symmetry is
unbroken
in these vs-vacua, this results,
as in section 41.1, in
SU(N_{c})\rightarrow SU({\overline{N}}_{c})\times U^{(1)}(1)\times U^{2N_{c}-N_{F}-1}(1)
, and main contributions
\sim\Lambda_{2}
to particle masses
originate now from this higgsing of
X^{adj}_{SU(N_{c})}
, see
(
41.1.1
),(
41.1.16
),

\displaystyle\langle\,X^{adj}_{SU(N_{c})}\rangle=\langle X^{adj}_{SU({\overline{N}}_{c})}+X^{(1)}_{U(1)}+X^{adj}_{SU(2N_{c}-N_{F})}\rangle,

(45.2.1)

\langle\sqrt{2}X^{adj}_{SU(2N_{c}-N_{F})}\rangle=C_{2N_{c}-N_{F}}\Lambda_{2}\,{\rm diag}\,\Bigl(\,\underbrace{0}_{{\overline{N}}_{c}}\,;\,\underbrace{\omega^{0},\,\omega^{1},\,...\,\omega^{2N_{c}-N_{F}-1}}_{2N_{c}-N_{F}}\,\Bigr)\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\omega=\exp\{\frac{2\pi i}{2N_{c}-N_{F}}\}\,,

\sqrt{2}\,X^{(1)}_{U(1)}=a_{1}\,{\rm diag}(\,\underbrace{\,1}_{{\overline{N}}_{c}}\,;\,\underbrace{\,c_{1}}_{2N_{c}-N_{F}}),\,c_{1}=-\,\frac{{\overline{N}}_{c}}{2N_{c}-N_{F}},\,\,\langle Q^{i}_{k}\rangle=\langle{\overline{Q}}_{i}^{\,k}\rangle=0,\,\,k={\overline{N}}_{c}+1...\,N_{c},\,i=1...\,N_{F}.

Now, vice versa, higgsed
\langle X^{A}_{SU(2N_{c}-N_{F})}\rangle\sim\Lambda_{2}

prevent
quarks
Q^{i}_{k},\,{\overline{Q}}^{\,k}_{i},\,k={\overline{N}}_{c}+1...\,N_{c},\,i=1...\,N_{F}

\langle Q^{i}_{k}\rangle=\langle{\overline{Q}}_{i}^{\,k}\rangle=0

),
\langle a_{0}\rangle=m,\,\langle S\rangle_{N_{c}}=0
. The physical reason is
that   these quarks acquire now masses
\sim\Lambda_{2}
, much larger than
a potentially possible scale
\sim(\mu_{\rm x}m)^{1/2}
of their coherent

45.1.1
). These quarks are too heavy and too
short ranged now and it looks unrealistic that they can form the
coherent condensate with
\langle Q^{i}_{k}\rangle=(\mu_{\rm x}\,m)^{1/2}
as before in section 45.1.

Besides, for independently moving heavy
{\overline{Q}}
and
Q
, their
physical (i.e. path dependent) phases induced by interactions with the

light

U^{2N_{c}-N_{F}}(1)
photons fluctuate
independently and
freely
at least at all scales
\mu>\mu^{\rm low}_{\rm cut}=(\rm several){\rm max}\{(\mu_{\rm x}m)^{1/2},\,(\mu_{\rm x}\Lambda_{2})^{1/2}\}
. I.e., in any
case,
\langle Q^{i}_{k}\rangle_{\mu^{\rm low}_{\rm cut}}=\langle{\overline{Q}}_{i}^{\,k}\rangle_{\mu^{\rm low}_{\rm cut}}=0
. Clearly, this concerns
also all other heavy charged particles with masses
\sim\Lambda_{2}
.
Therefore, after integrating out the theory (
45.1
) over the
interval from the high energy down to the scale
\mu^{\rm low}_{\rm cut}
, all particles with masses
\sim\Lambda_{2}

decouple
as heavy
already at the scale
\mu<\Lambda_{2}/(\rm several)
, i.e. much above

\mu^{\rm low}_{\rm cut}
, and definitely
the heavy quarks by
themselves do not give any contributions to masses of remaining
lighter degrees of freedom in the Lagrangian (
45.2.2
)
because

\langle Q^{i}_{k}\rangle_{\mu^{\rm low}_{\rm cut}}=\langle{\overline{Q}}_{i}^{\,k}\rangle_{\mu^{\rm low}_{\rm cut}}=0
.
And after the heavy particles decoupled at
\mu<\Lambda_{2}/(\rm several)
,
they themselves do not affect the lower energy theory. This is exactly
the same situation as with heavy quarks with masses
\sim\Lambda_{2}

discussed in detail in section 41.1 (and qualitatively the same as
with
X^{adj}_{SU({\overline{N}}_{c})}
with masses
\sim\mu_{\rm x}\gg(\mu_{\rm x}m)^{1/2}
in
section 41.4), and only the parameter
m\gg\Lambda_{2}
in this section while

m\ll\Lambda_{2}
in sections 41.1 and 41.4, and always
\mu_{\rm x}\ll\Lambda_{2}
.

The lower energy original electric
{\cal N}=2\,\,SU({\overline{N}}_{c})
theory at
the scale
\mu=\mu^{\rm low}_{\rm cut}\ll\Lambda_{2}
, with

and

\sim\mu_{\rm x}a_{0}^{2}\,
,

\displaystyle{\mathcal{W}}_{N_{c}}={\mathcal{W}}_{\,{\overline{N}}_{c}}+{\mathcal{W}}_{D}+{\mathcal{W}}_{a_{0},\,a_{1}}\,,

{\mathcal{W}}_{\,{\overline{N}}_{c}}=(m-a_{0}-a_{1}){\rm Tr}\,({\overline{Q}}Q)_{{\overline{N}}_{c}}-{\rm Tr}\,\Bigl({\overline{Q}}\sqrt{2}X_{SU({\overline{N}}_{c})}^{\rm adj}Q\Bigr)_{{\overline{N}}_{c}}+\mu_{\rm x}(1+\delta_{2}){\rm Tr}\,(X^{\rm adj}_{SU({\overline{N}}_{c})})^{2}\,,

{\mathcal{W}}_{a_{0},\,a_{1}}=\frac{\mu_{\rm x}}{2}N_{c}\,a^{2}_{0}+\frac{\mu_{\rm x}}{2}\frac{{\overline{N}}_{c}N_{c}}{{\rm b}_{2}}\,(1+\delta_{1})\,a^{2}_{1}+\mu_{\rm x}N_{c}\delta_{3}\,a_{1}(m-a_{0}-c_{1}a_{1})+\mu_{\rm x}N_{c}\delta_{4}\,(m-a_{0}-c_{1}a_{1})^{2}\,,

Proceeding now similarly to section 41.1, we obtain from

\displaystyle\langle a_{0}\rangle=m\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\langle a_{1}\rangle=0\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\langle a_{D,j}\rangle=0\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\langle X_{SU({\overline{N}}_{c})}^{\rm adj}\rangle=0\,,

From (
45.2.3
), the multiplicity of these vs -vacua in this
phase is
N_{vs}=C^{{\overline{N}}_{c}}_{N_{F}}=C^{N_{c}}_{N_{F}}
as it should be, the

On the whole, the mass spectrum looks now as follows.

1) Due to higgsing of
X^{adj}_{SU(2N_{c}-N_{F})}
at the scale
\sim\Lambda_{2}
,

\,\,SU(N_{c})\rightarrow SU({\overline{N}}_{c})\times U^{(1)}(1)\times U^{2N_{c}-N_{F}-1}(1)
, there
is   a large number of original pure electrically
SU(2N_{c}-N_{F})
charged
particles with the largest masses
\sim\Lambda_{2}
. They all are weakly
confined due to higgsing at the scale
\mu\sim(\mu_{\rm x}m)^{1/2}
of
mutually non-local with them
2N_{c}-N_{F}
BPS dyons
D_{j},\overline{D}_{j}
with the

\sigma^{1/2}_{D}\sim\langle{\overline{D}}_{j}D_{j}\rangle^{1/2}\sim(\mu_{\rm x}m)^{1/2}\ll\Lambda_{2}
. These original electrically charged particles form
hadrons with the mass scale
\sim\Lambda_{2}\,
. Clearly, higgsed
\langle X^{adj}_{SU(2N_{c}-N_{F})}\rangle\sim\Lambda_{2}
does not break
by itself
the
global flavor symmetry. All heavy quarks with
SU(2N_{c}-N_{F})
colors have
equal masses
|C_{2N_{c}-N_{F}}\Lambda_{2}|
and are in the (anti)fundamental
representation of the non-Abelian
SU(N_{F})
, they form e.g. a number
of
SU(N_{F})
adjoint hadrons with masses
\sim\Lambda_{2}
. Original
SU(2N_{c}-N_{F})

adjoint heavy gluons and scalars are flavorless and have different
masses
|\Lambda_{j}-\Lambda_{k}|,\,\Lambda_{j}=C_{2N_{c}-N_{F}}\Lambda_{2}\omega^{j-1},\,\omega=\exp\{2\pi i/(2N_{c}-N_{F})\}
.
There is no color-flavor locking in
this heaviest sector
:  the global
U(N_{F})
is unbroken while color

SU(2N_{c}-N_{F})\rightarrow U^{2N_{c}-N_{F}-1}(1)
.

2) Due to higgsing of these dyons
D_{j},{\overline{D}}_{j}
, there are
2N_{c}-N_{F}

long
{\cal N}=2\,\,U(1)
multiplets of massive photons, all with
masses
\sim\langle{\overline{D}}_{j}D_{j}\rangle^{1/2}\sim(\mu_{\rm x}m)^{1/2}
.

3) There are
{\overline{N}}_{c}^{\,2}
long
{\cal N}=2
multiplets of massive
gluons with masses
\sim\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle^{1/2}_{{\overline{N}}_{c}}\sim(\mu_{\rm x}m)^{1/2}\ll\Lambda_{2}
due to higgsing of
{\overline{N}}_{c}
flavors of original electric
quarks with
SU({\overline{N}}_{c})
colors.

4)
2{\rm n}_{1}{\rm n}_{2}=2{\overline{N}}_{c}N_{c}
(complex) Nambu-Goldstone multiplets are
massless (in essence, these are remained original electric quarks
with
{\rm n}_{2}=N_{c}=N_{F}-{\overline{N}}_{c}
flavors and
SU({\overline{N}}_{c})
colors). There are
no other massless particles at
\mu_{\rm x}\neq 0,\,m\neq 0
.

5) There are no particles with masses
\sim m\gg\Lambda_{2}
.

The
U(N_{c})
curve (
40.2
) has in this phase at
\mu_{\rm x}\rightarrow 0

{\overline{N}}_{c}
equal double roots
e_{k}=-m
corresponding to
{\overline{N}}_{c}
out of

N_{F}
higgsed original electric quarks with
SU({\overline{N}}_{c})
colors, and

2N_{c}-N_{F}
unequal double roots
e_{j}=-m+\omega^{j-1}\Lambda_{2}

corresponding to dyons
D_{j}
.

It is worth recalling once more that higgsed
\langle\,X^{adj}_{SU(2N_{c}-N_{F})}\rangle\sim\Lambda_{2}
in (
45.2.1
)
does not
break by itself the global flavor symmetry, it is really broken only
in the
SU({\overline{N}}_{c})
color sector by higgsed
{\overline{N}}_{c}
quarks with
N_{F}

flavors and
SU({\overline{N}}_{c})
colors
.

In essence, the situation here with the heavy not higgsed quarks

Q^{i}_{k},{\overline{Q}}^{\,k}_{i},\,i=1...N_{F},\,k={\overline{N}}_{c}+1...N_{c}
with masses

\sim\Lambda_{2}
and with their chiral bilinear condensates (really, it is
more adequate to speak about mean values, using the word
”condensate”   only for ”genuine”, i.e. coherent, condensates of
higgsed fields)
is the same
as in br2 vacua of section 41.1, see
(
41.1.16
)-(
41.1.26
) and accompanying text. In the lower
energy phase of this section at
\mu_{\rm x}\ll\Lambda_{2}^{2}/m
, the nonzero mean
values of
non-factorizable
local bilinears

\langle(({\overline{Q}}Q))_{1.2}\rangle_{2N_{c}-N_{F}}
of these heavy quarks with masses

\sim\Lambda_{2}
originate from: a) the quantum loop effects in the strong
coupling (and non-perturbative) regime at the scale
\mu\sim\Lambda_{2}
,
transforming these bilinears
operators
of heavy quark fields
into bilinears
operators
of light
SU({\overline{N}}_{c})
quark fields and the

operator

\Sigma_{D}
; b) and finally, from formed at much lower
scales
\mu\sim(\mu_{\rm x}m)^{1/2}\ll\Lambda_{2}
the genuine (i.e. coherent)
condensates of light higgsed quarks and dyons. I.e., the equations
(
41.1.17
)-(
41.1.19
) and
(
41.1.22
)-(
41.1.26
) (with
A=-2
) remain the same and
only the values of condensates are different in br2 and vs vacua, see
(
45.2.3
).

In this section, the corresponding values of condensates of heavy
quarks with
SU(2N_{c}-N_{F})
colors,
N_{F}
flavors, and masses
\sim\Lambda_{2}
in
these vs-vacua with
{\rm n}_{2}=N_{c},\,{\rm n}_{1}={\overline{N}}_{c}
, and
\langle S\rangle_{N_{c}}=0
differ from those in the br2 vacua with
{\rm n}_{2}>N_{c},\,{\rm n}_{1}<{\overline{N}}_{c}
and
\langle S\rangle_{N_{c}}\neq 0
in section 41.1 only by:
1)
\hat{m}\rightarrow m
due to
SU(N_{c})\rightarrow U(N_{c})
,   2) the absence of
non-leading power corrections originating in br2 vacua from the SYM
part, see (
45.3
),(
45.2.3
), compare with
(
45.1.1
),(
41.1.17
),(
41.1.19
),

\displaystyle\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{N_{c}}=[\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{2N_{c}-N_{F}}=\mu_{\rm x}m\,]+[\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{{\overline{N}}_{c}}=\sum_{a=1}^{{\overline{N}}_{c}}\langle{\overline{Q}}^{\,a}_{2}\rangle\langle Q^{2}_{a}\rangle=0\,]=\mu_{\rm x}m\,,

(45.2.4)

\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{N_{c}}=[\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{{\overline{N}}_{c}}=\langle{\overline{Q}}^{\,1}_{1}\rangle\langle Q^{1}_{1}\rangle=\mu_{\rm x}m\,]+[\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{2N_{c}-N_{F}}=-\mu_{\rm x}m\,]=0\,,

\langle{\rm Tr\,}({\overline{Q}}Q)\rangle_{2N_{c}-N_{F}}=N_{c}\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{2N_{c}-N_{F}}+{\overline{N}}_{c}\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{2N_{c}-N_{F}}=\langle\Sigma_{D}\rangle=(2N_{c}-N_{F})\mu_{\rm x}m\,,

while
\langle\sqrt{2}X^{adj}_{SU(2N_{c}-N_{F})}\rangle
in (
45.2.1
)
is the same as (
41.1.1
) and differs from (
45.1.1
).

There is one important qualitative difference between the vs vacua
and   br2 vacua. Because (
45.3
),
(
45.4
),(
45.2.2
),(
45.2.3
) remain the same at

m\lessgtr\Lambda_{2}
, and
\langle a_{0}\rangle=m
still ”eats” all quark
masses
"m"
in (
45.1
) at all
m\lessgtr\Lambda_{2}
, the whole physics
in vs-vacua is qualitatively independent separately of the ratio

m/\Lambda_{2}
and
depends only on a competition between
(\mu_{\rm x}m)^{1/2}

and
\Lambda_{2}
. For this reason, the lower energy phase with
m\gg\Lambda_{2},\,(\mu_{\rm x}m)^{1/2}\ll\Lambda_{2}
in vs-vacua of this section continues smoothly
into the region
\mu_{\rm x}\ll m\ll\Lambda_{2}
. This is in contrast with the br2
vacua with
\mu_{\rm x}\ll m\ll\Lambda_{2}
in section 41.1. In the latter, all
properties depend essentially on the ratio
m/\Lambda_{2}
and they evolve
into the qualitatively different br1 vacua of section 43 at

m\gg\Lambda_{2}
.

Comparing the mass spectra at
(\mu_{\rm x}m)^{1/2}\gg\Lambda_{2}
in section 45.1
with those in this section at
(\mu_{\rm x}m)^{1/2}\ll\Lambda_{2}
one can see the
qualitative difference between these two cases.
The lower energy
phase with
\mu_{\rm x}\ll\Lambda_{2}^{2}/m
in this section is, in essence, the phase
of the unbroken
{\cal N}=2
theory because it continues smoothly
down   to
\mu_{\rm x}\rightarrow 0
. While the higher energy phase of section 45.1 with

\mu_{\rm x}\gg\Lambda_{2}^{2}/m
, because it cannot be continued as it is down to

\mu_{\rm x}\rightarrow 0
, is, in essence, the phase of the strongly broken
{\cal N}=2
theory (i.e. already the genuine
{\cal N}=1
theory in this
sense)
. Remind that the applicability of the whole machinery with
the   use of roots of the curve (
40.2
) is guaranteed only at

\mu_{\rm x}\rightarrow 0
. I.e., it is applicable only to the lower energy phase of this
section, and clearly not applicable to the higher energy phase of
section 45.1, in spite of that
\mu_{\rm x}\ll\Lambda_{2}
therein also.

And these two phases at
\mu_{\rm x}\gtrless\Lambda_{2}^{2}/m
are qualitatively
different, compare e.g. (
45.1.1
) and (
45.2.1
). (In the
higher energy phase with

\langle{\overline{Q}}^{\,b}_{i=b+{\overline{N}}_{c}}\rangle=\langle Q^{i=b+{\overline{N}}_{c}}_{b}\rangle=(\mu_{\rm x}m)^{1/2},\,\,b=1...N_{c},\,\,i={\overline{N}}_{c}+1...N_{F}

and
\langle m-a_{0}\rangle=0,\,\langle S\rangle_{N_{c}}=0
, the mean
value
\langle X^{\rm adj}_{SU(N_{c})}\rangle\neq 0

is
incompatible with the Konishi anomaly in

(
45.2
),(
45.6
)). Besides, in this higher energy phase in
section 45.1 all charged and neutral gluons from
SU(2N_{c}-N_{F})
have
exactly equal masses
|g(\mu_{\rm x}m)^{1/2}|
in the whole wide region
from
(\mu_{\rm x}m)^{1/2}\gg\Lambda_{2}
down to
(\mu_{\rm x}m)^{1/2}>(\rm several)\Lambda_{2}
,
see (
45.1.1
). On the other hand, in the lower energy phase of
this section, the masses of charged
SU(2N_{c}-N_{F})
gluons differ greatly at

\,\mu_{\rm x}\rightarrow 0:\,\mu_{\rm gl}^{ij}\sim|(\omega^{i}-\omega^{j})\Lambda_{2}|\,,\,\,i\neq j,\,\,i,j=0...2N_{c}-N_{F}-1
, see (
45.2.1
), and differ
from masses of neutral gluons. This shows that the dependence of
masses of these gluons on
\mu_{\rm x}m
is non-analytic. The mean values
of
\langle X^{adj}_{SU(N_{c})}\rangle
and
\langle Q^{i}_{k}\rangle=\langle{\overline{Q}}_{i}^{\,k}\rangle,\,k={\overline{N}}_{c}+1...\,N_{c},\,i=1...\,N_{F}
also behave non-analytically, compare (
45.1.1
)
and(
45.2.1
). In this sense, we can say that there is a phase
transition somewhere at
(\mu_{\rm x}m)^{1/2}\sim\Lambda_{2}
. This is in
contradiction with a widespread opinion that there are no phase
transitions in the supersymmetric theories because the gauge
invariant   mean values of chiral fields ( e.g.
\langle(({\overline{Q}}Q))_{1,2}\rangle_{N_{c}},\,\langle S\rangle_{N_{c}},\,\,\langle{\rm Tr\,}(X^{adj}_{SU(N_{c})})^{2}\rangle
in (
45.2
)-(
45.4
))
depend holomorphically on the chiral superpotential parameters.

This phenomenon with the phase transition of the type described above
occurring at very small
\mu_{\rm x}\ll\Lambda_{2}
from the nearly unbroken
{\cal N}=2
at
\mu_{\rm x}\ll\Lambda_{2}^{2}/m
to the strongly broken
{\cal N}=2
at

\mu_{\rm x}\gg\Lambda_{2}^{2}/m
, i.e.
{\cal N}=2\rightarrow{\cal N}=1
, where the use of
roots of the curve (
40.2
) becomes not legitimate, appears
clearly
only
at
m\gg\Lambda_{2}
. Let us start e.g. with the nearly
unbroken
{\cal N}=2
theory at
sufficiently small

\mu_{\rm x}
where,
in particular, the whole machinery with the use of roots of the
curve (
40.2
) is still legitimate (for independent of
\mu_{\rm x}

quantities). In this section, when
\mu_{\rm x}
begins to increase, the
phase   transition   occurs at
\mu_{\rm x}\sim(\Lambda_{2}^{2}/m)\ll\Lambda_{2}
. But in
other cases,
see sections 46 and 47 below, it occurs even at parametrically smaller
values of
\mu_{\rm x}
than in this section. In br2 vacua of section 46 it
occurs at
\mu_{\rm x}\sim\langle\Lambda_{SU({\rm n}_{2})}\rangle^{2}/m,\,\langle\Lambda_{SU({\rm n}_{2})}\rangle\ll\Lambda_{2}
, see (
46.1.6
), and
in S- vacua of section 47 it occurs at
\mu_{\rm x}\sim\langle\Lambda_{SU(N_{F})}\rangle^{2}/m,\,\langle\Lambda_{SU(N_{F})}\rangle\ll\Lambda_{2}
, see (
47.4
).

Let us compare now the corresponding parts of unbroken non-Abelian
global symmetries of two phases, those in section 45.1 and here. It is

SU(N_{c})_{C+F}\times SU({\overline{N}}_{c})_{F}
of original pure electric particles in
section 45.1, where ”C+F” denotes the color-flavor locking. The higgsed
massive electric quarks
Q^{i=b+{\overline{N}}_{c}}_{b},{\overline{Q}}^{\,b}_{i=b+{\overline{N}}_{c}},\,\,b=1...N_{c},\,i={\overline{N}}_{c}+1...N_{F}
, with masses
m_{Q}\sim(\mu_{\rm x}m)^{1/2}\gg\Lambda_{2}
, together with massive electric
SU(N_{c})
gluons and

X^{adj}_{SU(N_{c})}
scalars form the long
{\cal N}=2
multiplet in
the adjoint representation of
SU(N_{c})_{C+F}
. The
2{\overline{N}}_{c}N_{c}

massless   Nambu-Goldstone multiplets are bifundamental, these are
original not higgsed quarks
Q^{i}_{a}
and
{\overline{Q}}^{a}_{i}
with
{\overline{N}}_{c}

flavors and
N_{c}
colors.

The corresponding non-Abelian global symmetry in this section is

SU({\overline{N}}_{c})_{C+F}\times SU(N_{c})_{F}
in the lighter sector, and higgsed
massive electric quarks
Q^{i=a}_{a},{\overline{Q}}^{\,a}_{i=a},\,\,a,i=1...{\overline{N}}_{c}
, with masses
m_{Q}\sim(\mu_{\rm x}m)^{1/2}\ll\Lambda_{2}
, together with

SU({\overline{N}}_{c})
gluons and
X^{adj}_{SU({\overline{N}}_{c})}
scalars form the long
{\cal N}=2
multiplet in the adjoint representation of
SU({\overline{N}}_{c})_{C+F}
, see
the point ”3” above. The
2{\overline{N}}_{c}N_{c}
massless Nambu-Goldstone
multiplets are also bifundamental. But, in addition,
all
N_{F}

flavors of original electric quarks with
SU(2N_{c}-N_{F})
colors have equal
large masses
\sim\Lambda_{2}
, so that the global
SU(N_{F})
is unbroken in
this heavy sector. In the limit
\mu_{\rm x}\rightarrow 0
these heavy quarks are not
confined and form the (anti)fundamental representation of
SU(N_{F})
.
All original unconfined at
\mu_{\rm x}\rightarrow 0

\,SU(2N_{c}-N_{F})
-adjoint and hybrid
gluons and scalars with the large masses
\sim\Lambda_{2}
are clearly flavor
singlets. At small
\mu_{\rm x}\neq 0
all these heavy pure electrically
charged particles are weakly confined and form hadrons with masses

\sim\Lambda_{2}
, but this confinement does not break
by itself

SU(N_{F})
. In particular, the heavy quarks form a number of
SU(N_{F})

adjoint hadrons.

Besides, it is worth noting the following. It is erroneous to imagine
that, because
\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{N_{c}}=\mu_{\rm x}\,m
still in the
\mu_{\rm x}\,m\ll\Lambda_{2}^{2}
phase of this section, then all quarks with
n_{2}=N_{c}

flavors are still higgsed in this lower energy phase as previously in
section 45.1 at
\mu_{\rm x}m\gg\Lambda_{2}^{2}
, i.e.
\langle{\overline{Q}}^{\,b}_{i=b+{\overline{N}}_{c}}\rangle=\langle Q^{i=b+{\overline{N}}_{c}}_{b}\rangle=(\mu_{\rm x}\,m)^{1/2},\,\,b=1...N_{c},\,\,i={\overline{N}}_{c}+1...N_{F}
, and so give corresponding
contributions to particle masses, but because
(\mu_{\rm x}m)^{1/2}\ll\Lambda_{2}

now, this will be of little importance. It is sufficient to notice
that, with
\langle X^{\rm adj}_{SU(2N_{c}-N_{F})}\rangle\sim\Lambda_{2}\neq 0
now,
see (
45.2.1
), this variant with still higgsed quarks with all

N_{c}
colors is incompatible with the Konishi anomalies in
(
45.2
),(
45.6
).

The difference of adjoint representations

SU(N_{c})_{C+F}\leftrightarrow SU({\overline{N}}_{c})_{C+F}
at
\mu_{\rm x}m\gtrless\Lambda_{2}^{2}

was considered e.g. in
[
42
,
41
]
as an evidence that all

SU({\overline{N}}_{c})
particles cannot be the same original pure electric
particles and, by analogy with
[
37
]
, it was finally claimed that
they all are dyons. E.e., the dyonic quarks
{\overline{d}}^{\,b}_{i},\,d^{i}_{b}

with
N_{F}
flavors of this
SU({\overline{N}}_{c})
are composites of original
flavored quarks with
SU({\overline{N}}_{c})
colors and corresponding flavorless
magnetic monopoles.

This line of reasonings is in a clear contradiction with the picture
described above in this section. The reason for different adjoint
representations is that the phase and mass spectrum are determined by

N_{c}
out of
N_{F}
higgsed quarks of
SU(N_{c})
at
\mu_{\rm x}\,m\gg\Lambda_{2}^{2}

(and
X^{adj}_{SU(N_{c})}
is not higgsed,

\langle X^{adj}_{SU(N_{c})}\rangle=0
, see (
45.1.1
)), while they
are determined by higgsed both
X^{\rm adj}_{SU(2N_{c}-N_{F})}
and

{\overline{N}}_{c}
out of
N_{F}
quarks of
SU({\overline{N}}_{c})
at
\mu_{\rm x}m\ll\Lambda_{2}^{2}
, see
(
45.2.1
),(
45.2.3
), (both phases in section 45.1 and here
at
m\gg\Lambda_{2}
, and the case
\mu_{\rm x}\ll m\ll\Lambda_{2}
clearly corresponds to
the   regime
\mu_{\rm x}m<\Lambda_{2}^{2}
, see section 45.3 below).

Besides, the above described in this section picture with the
phase transition
at
\mu_{\rm x}m\gtrless\Lambda_{2}^{2}
(to avoid entering the
unphysical regime with
g^{2}(\mu<\Lambda_{2})<0
without higgsed
\langle X^{adj}_{SU(2N_{c}-N_{F})}\rangle\sim\Lambda_{2}
), is in a clear contradiction with
the proposed by M.Shifman and A.Yung
a smooth analytic
crossover
from the region
\mu_{\rm x}m\gg\Lambda_{2}^{2}
to the
”instead-of-confinement” regime   in the region
\mu_{\rm x}\,m\ll\Lambda_{2}^{2}
, with
still unbroken the
non-Abelian

SU(N_{c})_{C+F}
global symmetry as it was
in section 45.1 above, see e.g.
[
42
,
41
]
. And the
smooth analytic crossover implies that

N_{c}
of
N_{F}
original
SU(N_{c})
quarks are
still higgsed
,
see (
45.1.1
), and only the value of their condensate
is smaller now,

\displaystyle\langle{\overline{Q}}_{i=b+{\overline{N}}_{c}}^{\,b}Q^{i=b+{\overline{N}}_{c}}_{b}\rangle=\langle{\overline{Q}}_{i=b+{\overline{N}}_{c}}^{\,b}\rangle\langle Q^{i=b+{\overline{N}}_{c}}_{b}\rangle=(\mu_{\rm x}m)^{1/2}\ll\Lambda_{2}\,,\hskip 11.74988ptb=1...N_{c}\,,\hskip 11.74988pti={\overline{N}}_{c}+1...N_{F}\,.

(45.2.5)

At
\mu_{\rm x}m\ll\Lambda_{2}^{2}
, according to M.Shifman and A.Yung, see e.g.

[
48
]
and refs therein to their previous related papers. -

a) There is a large number of light particles with masses
\ll\Lambda_{2}

which all are dyonic solitons. The first part of this set is the

SU({\overline{N}}_{c})
gauge theory with
N_{F}
flavors of above dyonic quarks

d^{i}_{b},\,{\overline{d}}^{\,b}_{i},\,i=1...N_{F},\,b=1...{\overline{N}}_{c}
.
\,{\overline{N}}_{c}
them of

N_{F}
are higgsed at small
\mu_{\rm x}\neq 0
and, together with dyonic

SU({\overline{N}}_{c})
gluons and scalars, form the adjoint representation of the
unbroken
SU({\overline{N}}_{c})_{C+F}
global symmetry. The second part of this set
includes
2N_{c}{\overline{N}}_{c}
massless Nambu-Goldstone particles which are
remained non-higgsed dyonic quarks
d^{i}_{b},\,{\overline{d}}^{\,b}_{i},\,i={\overline{N}}_{c}+1...N_{F},\,b=1...{\overline{N}}_{c}
. The third part includes
U^{2N_{c}-N_{F}}(1)

photon multiplets and
2N_{c}-N_{F}
dyons
\overline{\textsf{D}}_{n},\,{\textsf{D}}_{n}
which are composites of diagonal original quarks

{\overline{Q}}^{\,n}_{n},\,Q^{n}_{n},\,n=1...2N_{c}-N_{F}
and corresponding
SU(2N_{c}-N_{F})

magnetic monopoles :
{\textsf{D}}_{n}=(Q^{n}_{n}+M_{n})
. All quark-like
dyons (except for Nambu-Goldstone particles) are higgsed at

\mu_{\rm x}\neq 0
.

As was emphasized in section 41.1, as a result of this picture,
the dyons

{\textsf{D}}^{i}_{n},\,n=1...2N_{c}-N_{F},\,i=1...N_{F}

will
really realize the fundamental representation
N_{F}
of the flavor

SU(N_{F})
and this will result in a wrong pattern of the flavor
symmetry breaking at
\mu_{\rm x}\neq 0
. Besides, the number of such
dyons
{\textsf{D}}^{i}_{n}
will be too large.

b) At the same time, in the region
\mu_{\rm x}m\ll\Lambda_{2}^{2}
of this section

all original electric particles from the unbroken adjoint
representation of
SU(N_{c})_{C+F}
in section 45.1 mysteriously
acquire large equal masses

\sim\Lambda_{2}
(in addition to equal
contributions
\sim(\mu_{\rm x}m)^{1/2}
to their masses from still
higgsed quarks, see (
45.2.5
)).

It is not even attempted to recognize the dynamical mechanism
responsible for the origin of these large equal masses
\sim\Lambda_{2}
of
all original particles forming the long
{\cal N}=2
adjoint
representation of
SU(N_{c})_{C+F}
.

Let us recall that in the whole weak coupling region
\mu_{\rm x}\,m\gg\Lambda_{2}^{2}
,
with quarks higgsed as in (
45.1.1
), it follows from the Konishi
anomalies (
45.2
),(
45.6
) (valid for each flavor
separately) that all
\langle X^{A}\rangle=0,\,A=1...N_{c}^{2}-1
. If,
according to M.Shifman and A.Yung, the transition from
\mu_{\rm x}m\gg\Lambda_{2}^{2}
to
\mu_{\rm x}\,m\ll\Lambda_{2}^{2}
is a smooth analytic crossover, then
all
\langle X^{A}\rangle=0
at
\mu_{\rm x}m\ll\Lambda_{2}^{2}
also and quarks are
higgsed still as in (
45.1.1
). From where then appear large
masses
\sim\Lambda_{2}
of all original particles forming the adjoint
representation of
SU(N_{c})_{C+F}
? This contradicts the BPS
properties of original particles.

And it is not attempted to answer the question: are the properties of
this mysterious mechanism responsible for the appearance of these
large masses
\sim\Lambda_{2}
compatible with the smooth analytic crossover
and with the unbroken global
SU(N_{c})_{C+F}
symmetry ? Recall that
the described above in this section concrete dynamical mechanism
responsible for the appearance of large masses
\sim\Lambda_{2}
of all
original
charged
particles with
SU(2N_{c}-N_{F})
colors, i.e. higgsed

\langle X^{adj}_{SU(2N_{c}-N_{F})}\rangle\sim\Lambda_{2}
(
45.2.1
), is
incompatible with the unbroken global
SU(N_{c})_{C+F}
requiring
equal masses of all
N_{c}^{2}-1

\,SU(N_{c})_{C+F}
adjoint particles.
E.g., charged
SU(2N_{c}-N_{F})
adjoint gluons have different masses.

Besides, as explained in detail in section 41.1, e.g. the quarks

Q^{i}_{a},\,{\overline{Q}}^{a}_{i},\,a=1...2N_{c}-N_{F},\,i=1...N_{F}
with masses
\sim\Lambda_{2}

at
(\mu_{\rm x}m)^{1/2}\ll\Lambda_{2}
are too heavy and too short ranged and
cannot form the coherent condensate with
\langle Q\rangle_{\Lambda_{2}/(\rm several)}=\langle{\overline{Q}}\rangle_{\Lambda_{2}/(\rm several)}\sim(\mu_{\rm x}\,m)^{1/2}
, because they decouple already at the scale
\sim\Lambda_{2}/(\rm several)\gg(\mu_{\rm x}\,m)^{1/2}
where
\langle Q\rangle_{\Lambda_{2}/(\rm several)}=\langle{\overline{Q}}\rangle_{\Lambda_{2}/(\rm several)}=0
and do not
affect by themselves further lower energy evolution of remained
lighter fields. In other words, as opposed to (
45.2.5
), the
nonzero total mean values of heavy quarks bilinears

\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{2N_{c}-N_{F}}
and
\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{2N_{c}-N_{F}}
in
(
45.2.4
) are non-factorizable. As explained in detail in
section 41.1, they are induced by nonzero genuine condensates of light
higgsed quarks with
SU({\overline{N}}_{c})
colors and light dyons
{\overline{D}}_{j},D_{j}
.
Moreover, e.g. the appearance at
(\mu_{\rm x}m)^{1/2}\ll\Lambda_{2}
of the quark
mass terms
\sim\Lambda_{2}\sum_{a,i=1}^{N_{c}}{\overline{Q}}^{a}_{i}Q^{i}_{a}
in the
superpotential contradicts the unbroken in these vs-vacua
Z_{2N_{c}-N_{F}}

symmetry, and also the mass terms
\sim\Lambda_{2}{\rm Tr}(X^{adj}_{SU(N_{c})})^{2}
of all
N_{c}^{2}-1
scalars in the superpotential
contradict both the unbroken
U_{R}(1)
and
Z_{2N_{c}-N_{F}}
symmetries. The
very idea that original quarks acquired their masses
\sim\Lambda_{2}
at

\mu_{\rm x}\rightarrow 0
not from higgsed
\langle X^{adj}_{SU(2N_{c}-N_{F})}\rangle\sim\Lambda_{2}

but from some (unrecognized) outside sources contradicts their BPS
properties. Recall that in similar br2 vacua in section 41.1, with
higgsed
\langle X^{\rm adj}_{SU(2N_{c}-N_{F})}\rangle\sim\Lambda_{2}
in
(
41.1.1
), the condensates of light higgsed original quarks

\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle=\langle{\overline{Q}}^{1}_{1}\rangle\langle Q^{1}_{1}\rangle

in (
41.1.4
) agree with
independently
calculated their
values from roots of the curve (
40.2
) (and the same in
vs-vacua   considered   here with
{\rm n}_{1}={\overline{N}}_{c}
). And because the
values calculated from roots are valid only for BPS particles,

this confirms the BPS properties of original quarks
.

c) In addition, in this variant with the smooth analytic crossover,
all dyons
d^{k}_{b},\,k,b=1...{\overline{N}}_{c}
,
{\textsf{D}}_{n},\,n=1...2N_{c}-N_{F}

and still quarks

Q^{i}_{a},\,a=1...N_{c},\,i={\overline{N}}_{c}+1...N_{F}
are
higgsed simultaneously
with
\langle d\rangle\sim\langle Q\rangle\sim(\mu_{\rm x}m)^{1/2}\,,\,\langle{\textsf{D}}\rangle\sim\max[(\mu_{\rm x}\Lambda_{2})^{1/2},\,(\mu_{\rm x}m)^{1/2}]
, see e.g. (
45.2.5
). But, in
any case, these quarks
Q^{i}_{a}
are definitely mutually non-local with
respect to these dyons. How then can it be?

Comparing properties of proposed in
[
37
,
42
,
41
,
48
]
the smooth
analytic crossover between regions
\mu_{\rm x}m\gtrless\Lambda_{2}^{2}
(with the
”instead-of-confinement” regime in the region
\mu_{\rm x}\,m\ll\Lambda_{2}^{2}
), and
those with the phase transition described above in this section, it is   seen
that these properties are, so to say, ”orthogonal” to each other.   And,
on account of critical remarks presented above, we consider the
proposal of the smooth analytic crossover and the
”instead-of-confinement” regime as not self-consistent.

In addition, we would like to especially emphasize (and this concerns
the whole content of this paper, not only those of this section) that,   for

higgsed fields giving nonzero contributions to particle masses
,

the mean values of fields themselves should be nonzero
,
i.e.
\langle Q\rangle=\langle{\overline{Q}}\rangle\neq 0
or
\langle X\rangle\neq 0
,
not only
something like
\langle Q^{\dagger}Q\rangle^{1/2}\neq 0
or
\langle X^{\dagger}X\rangle^{1/2}\neq 0

for the D-terms, or
\langle{\overline{Q}}Q\rangle^{1/2}\neq 0,\,\langle X^{2}\rangle^{1/2}\neq 0
for the F-terms (which are, as a rule, nonzero
even for those fields which are really not higgsed).

This is especially clearly seen e.g. from the mass terms of fermionic
superpartners. For the D-terms of
higgsed
quarks or
X
these
look as:

59
59

59

Really
\langle...\rangle
in (
45.2.6
),(
45.2.7
) have to be
understood as
\langle...\rangle_{\mu^{low}_{cut}=M/(\rm several)}
, where
M
is the pole mass of
Q,\,{\overline{Q}}
or
X
.

\displaystyle{\rm Tr\,}\Bigl[\,\langle Q^{\,\dagger}\rangle\lambda\chi+{\overline{\chi}}\lambda\langle{\overline{Q}}^{\,\dagger}\rangle\,\,\Bigr]\hskip 11.74988pt{\rm\,or}\hskip 11.74988pt{\rm Tr\,}\Bigl(\,\langle X^{\dagger}\rangle\,[\psi,\lambda]\,\Bigr)\,,

(45.2.6)

and for the F-terms they look as

\displaystyle{\rm Tr\,}\Bigl[\,{\overline{\chi}}\psi\langle Q\rangle+\langle{\overline{Q}}\rangle\psi\chi\,\Bigr]\hskip 11.74988pt{\rm or}\hskip 11.74988pt{\rm Tr\,}\Bigl[\,{\overline{\chi}}\langle X\rangle\chi\,\Bigr]\,.

(45.2.7)

45.3

m\ll\Lambda_{2}

Comparing with the case
\mu_{\rm x}\ll\Lambda_{2},\,\,m\gg\Lambda_{2}
in
sections 45.1-45.2, the case
\mu_{\rm x}\ll m\ll\Lambda_{2}
corresponds clearly the
regime
\mu_{\rm x}\ll\Lambda_{2}^{2}/m
. The quark condensates remain the same as in
(
45.2.3
), and
\langle a_{0}\rangle=m
remains the same as in
(
45.2.3
) and still ”eats” all quark masses
"m"
.
The
phase remains the same as in section
45.2, and at
m\ll\Lambda_{2}
the
difference with the case
m\gg\Lambda_{2},\,\,\mu_{\rm x}\ll\Lambda_{2}^{2}/m
in section 45.2
is only quantitative, not qualitative, see (
45.2.3
). The main
difference is that
\langle D_{j}\rangle\sim(\mu_{\rm x}m)^{1/2}\gg(\mu_{\rm x}\Lambda_{2})^{1/2}
at
m\gg\Lambda_{2}
, while
\langle D_{j}\rangle\sim(\mu_{\rm x}\Lambda_{2})^{1/2}\gg(\mu_{\rm x}m)^{1/2}
at
m\ll\Lambda_{2}
. Still, there are no
particles with masses
\sim m
, the only particle masses
independent of
\mu_{\rm x}
are
\sim\Lambda_{2}
.

45.4

N_{F}=N_{c}

Now, in short about the vs vacuum with
N=N_{F}=N_{c}={\rm n}_{2},\,{\overline{N}}_{c}=0={\rm n}_{1}

at
m\lessgtr\Lambda_{2}
and
\mu_{\rm x}\ll\Lambda_{2}
. There is only one such vacuum. And
really, there is nothing extraordinary with this case.

45.4.1

Higher energy phase at
(\mu_{\rm x}m)^{1/2}\gg\Lambda_{2}

As always in all vs vacua, see (
45.3
),(
45.4
),

\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)\rangle_{N_{c}}=\mu_{\rm x}m,\,\langle S\rangle_{N_{c}}=0
in this
vacuum, and
\langle a_{0}\rangle=m
”eats” all quark masses. The whole
color group
U(N_{c})
is broken in this higher energy phase as before
in vs-vacua of section 45.1 by higgsed quarks at the scale
\mu\sim(\mu_{\rm x}m)^{1/2}\gg\Lambda_{2}
in the weak coupling regime, while
\langle X^{adj}_{SU(N_{c})}\rangle=0
is not higgsed, see (
45.1.1
).
All
SU(N_{c})
gluons have equal masses
\sim(\mu_{\rm x}m)^{1/2}
in this
phase in the whole wide region from
(\mu_{\rm x}m)^{1/2}\gg\Lambda_{2}
down to

(\mu_{\rm x}m)^{1/2}>(\rm several)\Lambda_{2}
. There is the residual global
non-Abelian
SU(N)_{C+F}
symmetry (i.e. color-flavor locking).

N^{2}-1
long
{\cal N}=2
multiplets of massive gluons are formed in
the adjoint representation of this remained unbroken global symmetry

SU(N)_{C+F}
. There are no particles with masses
\sim m
.

The global
SU(N)_{F}
is substituted by global
SU(N)_{C+F}
, so that
there are no massless Nambu-Goldstone particles in this vacuum.
And there are no massless particles at all in this phase.

45.4.2

Lower energy phase at
(\mu_{\rm x}m)^{1/2}\ll\Lambda_{2}

As in all vs-vacua, the total mean value of higgsed
a_{0}=\langle a_{0}\rangle^{(\rm several)m}_{m/(\rm several)}+[\,{\hat{a}}_{0}^{\rm soft}\,]^{m/(\rm several)}
originates and saturates at
\mu\sim m

and ”eats” masses ”m” of all quarks

\displaystyle\langle a_{0}\rangle=\frac{\langle{\,\rm Tr\,}({\overline{Q}}Q)\rangle_{N_{c}}}{N_{c}\mu_{\rm x}}=m\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\langle a_{0}\rangle=[\,\langle a_{0}\rangle^{(\rm several)\,m}_{m/(\rm several)}=m\,]+[\,\langle{\hat{a}}_{0}^{\rm\,soft}\rangle^{m/(\rm several)}_{\mu_{\rm cut}^{\rm lowest}=0}=0\,]\,.

(45.4.1)

In this phase, at all
m\gtrless\Lambda_{2}
, to avoid entering the unphysical
regime
g^{2}(\mu<\Lambda_{2})<0
in this UV free theory, the non-Abelian color

SU(N_{c})
is also broken in the strong coupling and non-perturbative
regime at the scale
\mu\sim\Lambda_{2}
by higgsed
\langle X^{adj}_{SU(N_{c})}\rangle\sim\Lambda_{2},\,SU(N_{c})\rightarrow U^{N_{c}-1}(1)
,
see (
41.1.1
),(
45.2.1
),

\displaystyle X^{adj}_{SU(N_{c})}=\langle X^{adj}_{SU(N_{c})}\rangle^{(\rm\,several)\Lambda_{2}}_{\Lambda_{2}/(\rm several)}+[\,{\hat{X}}^{adj,\rm\,soft}_{SU(N_{c})}\,]^{\Lambda_{2}/(\rm\,several)}\,,

(45.4.2)

\langle\sqrt{2}X^{adj}_{SU(N_{c})}\rangle^{(\rm several)\Lambda_{2}}_{\Lambda_{2}/(\rm\,several)}=C_{N}\Lambda_{2}\,{\rm\,diag}\,\Bigl(\,\rho^{0},\,\rho^{1},\,...,\,\rho^{N_{c}-1}\,\Bigr),\hskip 11.74988pt\rho=\exp\{\,\frac{2\pi i}{N_{c}}\,\}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\langle{\hat{X}}^{adj,\rm\,soft}_{SU(N_{c})}\rangle^{\Lambda_{2}/(\rm several)}_{\mu_{\rm\,cut}^{\rm\,lowest}=0}=0\,.

The unbroken non-trivial discrete symmetry is
Z_{2N_{c}-N_{F}}=Z_{N\geq 2}
in this vacuum, and it determines the standard pattern of this
color symmetry breaking by higgsed
\langle X^{adj}_{SU(N_{c})}\rangle

giving the largest contributions
\sim\Lambda_{2}
to particle masses. All
original charged particles acquire masses
\sim\Lambda_{2}
, while
N_{c}=N

light flavorless BPS dyons
D_{j}
(massless at
\mu_{\rm x}\rightarrow 0
) are formed
at the scale
\mu\sim\Lambda_{2}
.

Clearly, higgsed flavorless
X^{adj}_{SU(N_{c})}
does not break by
itself the flavor symmetry
SU(N_{F})
, i.e.
there is no
color-flavor locking
. The global non-Abelian flavor symmetry

SU(N_{F})=SU(N)_{F}
remains unbroken, while the non-Abelian color

SU(N_{c})=SU(N)_{C}
is broken down to Abelian one.

As before in section 45.2 for heavy quarks with
SU(2N_{c}-N_{F})
colors,
all quarks have equal masses
, but they are too heavy and too short
ranged now and cannot form the coherent condensate. I.e., they are

not higgsed
in this phase. This is in accord with the Konishi
anomaly (
45.6
) (valid for each flavor separately) which
requires
\langle Q^{i}_{i}\rangle=\langle{\overline{Q}}^{\,i}_{i}\rangle=0,\,i=1...N
when
X^{adj}_{SU(N_{c})}
is higgsed as in (
45.4.2
).

All equal mass quarks are clearly
in the (anti)fundamental
representation
of unbroken global
SU(N)_{F}
, while
the
non-Abelian color
SU(N)_{C}
is broken at the scale

\mu\sim\Lambda_{2}
down to Abelian
U^{N-1}(1)
,
and masses of all charged flavorless gluons (and scalars)
differ greatly in this lower energy phase at
\mu_{\rm x}\rightarrow 0:\,\mu_{\rm gl}^{ij}\sim|(\rho^{i}-\rho^{j})\Lambda_{2}|\,,\,\,i\neq j,\,\,i,j=1...N
, see (
45.4.2
). There is the phase transition
at
(\mu_{\rm x}m)^{1/2}\sim\Lambda_{2}
.

After all heavy particles with masses
\sim\Lambda_{2}
decoupled at

\mu=\Lambda_{2}/(\rm several)
, the low energy superpotential looks as

\displaystyle{\mathcal{W}}_{N_{F}}={\mathcal{W}}_{D}+{\mathcal{W}}_{a}\,,

(45.4.3)

{\mathcal{W}}_{D}=(m-a_{0})\sum_{j=1}^{N}{\overline{D}}_{j}D_{j}-\sum_{j=1}^{N}a_{D,\,j}{\overline{D}}_{j}D_{j}-\mu_{\rm x}\Lambda_{2}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\rho^{\,{j-1}}a_{D,\,j}+\mu_{\rm x}L\,\Bigl(\sum_{j=1}^{N}a_{D,\,j}\Bigr)\,

{\mathcal{W}}_{a}=\frac{\mu_{\rm x}}{2}N\,a_{0}^{2}+\mu_{\rm x}N\,{\hat{\delta}}_{4}\,(m-a_{0})^{2}\,,\hskip 11.74988ptN=2N_{c}-N_{F}=N_{c}=N_{F}\,.

We would like to emphasize that all dyons in
(
41.1.5
),(
41.2.4
),(
42.4
),(
45.2.2
)
and (
45.4.3
) (and in (
46.2.2
),(
47.6
)
below) are
the same
particles, and only
corresponding values of
2N^{\prime}_{c}-N^{\prime}_{F}
in
different vacua may be different.

From (
45.4.3
)

\langle a_{0}\rangle=m\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\langle a_{D,\,j}\rangle=0\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\langle{\overline{D}}_{j}\rangle\langle D_{j}\rangle=-\mu_{\rm x}\Lambda_{2}\rho^{\,{j-1}}+\mu_{\rm x}\,m\,,

\displaystyle\langle\Sigma_{D}\rangle=\sum_{j=1}^{N}\langle{\overline{D}}_{j}\rangle\langle D_{j}\rangle=N\mu_{\rm x}\,m=\langle{\rm Tr\,}({\overline{Q}}Q)\rangle_{N_{c}}\,.

(45.4.4)

All
N
dyons in (
45.4.3
) are higgsed at small
\mu_{\rm x}
at the
scale
\mu_{D}\sim\langle{\overline{D}}_{j}D_{j}\rangle^{1/2},\,\mu_{D}\sim\max[(\mu_{\rm x}m)^{1/2},\\
(\mu_{\rm x}\Lambda_{2})^{1/2}]
(at
m\gg\Lambda_{2}
below in this section
for definiteness), in the weak coupling regime
g_{D}(\mu_{D})\ll 1
, and

N
long
{\cal N}=2
multiplets of massive photons are formed with
masses
\sim\mu_{D}\ll\Lambda_{2}
. All original heavy charged particles with
masses
\sim\Lambda_{2}
are weakly confined, the string tension is

\sigma^{1/2}\sim\langle{\overline{D}}_{j}D_{j}\rangle^{1/2}\ll\Lambda_{2}
. In
particular, the heavy flavored quarks form a number of
SU(N_{F})

adjoint hadrons with masses
\sim\Lambda_{2}
. There are no massless
Nambu-Goldstone particles because the global flavor symmetry

U(N_{F})
is unbroken.

As for non-factorizable condensates of heavy non-higgsed in this lower
energy phase quarks with masses
\sim\Lambda_{2}
, (
41.1.16
) looks
similarly here, with
(2N_{c}-N_{F})\rightarrow N_{c}
, as well as (
41.1.18
).
Instead of (
41.1.20
) we have here

\displaystyle[\,{\rm Tr\,}(({\overline{Q}}Q))_{N_{c}}\,]^{(\rm several)\Lambda_{2}}_{(\rm several)(\mu_{\rm x}\Lambda_{2})^{1/2}}=\Bigl[\,{\hat{d}}_{1}N_{c}\mu_{\rm x}{\hat{a}}^{\rm soft}_{0}+\Sigma_{D}\,\Bigr]^{(\rm several)(\mu_{\rm x}\Lambda_{2})^{1/2}},\hskip 11.74988pt{\hat{d}}_{1}=O(1)\,.\,

(45.4.5)

This operator expansion leads to the numerical equality

\displaystyle\langle{\rm Tr\,}(({\overline{Q}}Q))_{N_{c}}\rangle^{(\rm\,several)\Lambda_{2}}_{\mu_{\rm\,cut}^{\rm\,lowest}=0}=\langle\Sigma_{D}\rangle=\langle\Sigma_{D}\rangle^{(\rm\,several)(\mu_{\rm x}\,m)^{1/2}}_{\mu_{\rm\,cut}^{\rm\,lowest}=0}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\langle{\hat{a}}^{\rm soft}_{0}\rangle^{\Lambda_{2}/(\rm\,several)}_{\mu_{\rm\,cut}^{\rm\,lowest}=0}=0\,,\hskip 11.74988ptm\gg\Lambda_{2}

(45.4.6)

as it should be, see (
45.4.1
),(
45.4.4
). As before in
section 45.2 for
\langle{\rm Tr\,}({\overline{Q}}Q)\rangle_{2N_{c}-N_{F}}
at
{\overline{N}}_{c}\geq 1
,
the nonzero total mean value of the heavy quark non-factorizable
bilinear operator originates and saturates not at the scale

\mu\sim\Lambda_{2}
, but only at much lower energies

\mu_{D}\sim(\mu_{\rm x}m)^{1/2}
.

Moreover, at
\mu_{\rm x}\rightarrow 0
, because
\langle a_{0}\rangle=m
”eats” all
quark masses, all charged solitons will have masses either
\sim\Lambda_{2}

or zero. But the
U(N_{c})
curve (
40.2
) has only
N_{c}
unequal
double roots
e^{D}_{j}=-m+\rho^{j-1}\Lambda_{2},\,j=1...N_{c}
in this
vacuum   corresponding to
N_{c}=N
massless BPS dyons
D_{j}
,
and shows that
there are no
additional charged
{\cal N}=2
BPS solitons
massless at
\mu_{\rm x}\rightarrow 0
. In particular, there are no massless pure
magnetic monopoles or other additional dyons, they all have masses

\sim\Lambda_{2}
. And there are no massless particles at all at small

\mu_{\rm x}\neq 0
. Besides, there are no particles with masses
\sim m\gg\Lambda_{2}
, the largest masses are
\sim\Lambda_{2}
.

According to e.g.
[
48
]
(and refs therein to their previous
related papers), the transition from
(\mu_{\rm x}\,m)^{1/2}\gg\Lambda_{2}
to

(\mu_{\rm x}\,m)^{1/2}\ll\Lambda_{2}
is a smooth analytical crossover with
still
higgsed quarks
,
\langle Q^{i}_{a}\rangle=\delta^{i}_{a}(\mu_{\rm x}\,m)^{1/2},\,i,a=1...N
,
and still unbroken
SU(N)_{C+F}
global symmetry
.
And all original particles from the long
{\cal N}=2\,\,SU(N)_{C+F}

adjoint multiplet mysteriously acquire equal masses
\sim\Lambda_{2}
at

(m\mu_{\rm x})^{1/2}<\Lambda_{2}
. Besides, e.g. each heavy original quark
Q^{i}

with the mysteriously
appeared mass
\sim\Lambda_{2}
, decays into the (flavored dyon-
flavorless antimonopole) pair
({\cal D}^{i}+{\overline{\cal M}})
(both
particles with masses
\sim\Lambda_{2}
) in the ”instead-of-confinement”
regime at
(\mu_{\rm x}\,m)^{1/2}<\Lambda_{2}
. Due to higgsed at small
\mu_{\rm x}\neq 0

light
dyons
\overline{\textsf{D}}_{n},\,\textsf{D}_{n},\,n=1...N

(composed, according to
[
48
]
, from diagonal quarks
Q^{n}_{n}
and
flavorless magnetic monopoles
M_{n}:\,\textsf{D}_{n}=(Q^{n}_{n}+M_{n})
),
these heavy
{\cal D}^{i}
and
{\overline{\cal M}}
are confined in this
”instead-of-confinement” regime, and this pair to which quark decays
forms a ”stringy meson” with the mass
\sim\Lambda_{2}
. This is, so to say,
”internal decay”, so that, as a whole, each pair
({\cal D}^{i}+{\overline{\cal M}})
remains the same quark
Q^{i}
.

Let us recall that, in any case, the considered theory is not at the
arbitrary point on the formal moduli space existing at the
mathematical point
\mu_{\rm x}\equiv 0
only, but stays in definite isolated
vacua - those at
\mu_{\rm x}\neq 0
, even in the formal limit
\mu_{\rm x}\rightarrow 0
. And
e.g. massive BPS quarks are at least marginally stable at
\mu_{\rm x}\rightarrow 0

in vacua considered in this paper and can in this case only formally
dissolve ”emitting” massless quanta.

And, in any case, similarly to section 45.2, it remains completely
unclear in the variant with the smooth crossover transition from

(m\mu_{\rm x})^{1/2}\gg\Lambda_{2}
to
(m\mu_{\rm x})^{1/2}\ll\Lambda_{2}
proposed in
[
48
]
:
a) from where mysteriously appeared large equal masses
\sim\Lambda_{2}
of
all particles from still unbroken long
{\cal N}=2\,\,SU(N)_{C+F}

adjoint multiplet (because, with still higgsed quarks
\langle Q^{i}_{a}\rangle=\delta^{i}_{a}(\mu_{\rm x}m)^{1/2},\,i,a=1...N
, the Konishi
anomaly (
45.6
) requires
\langle X^{adj}_{SU(N)}\rangle=0,\,A=1...N^{2}-1
);  b) because all
N^{2}-1\,\,SU(N)_{C+F}
adjoint gluons
acquired masses
\sim\Lambda_{2}
, from where then mysteriously appeared

U^{N-1}(1)
massless at
\mu_{\rm x}\rightarrow 0
photon multiplets;  c) how their
dyons
\textsf{D}_{n}=(Q^{n}_{n}+M_{n})
and mutually non-local with them
quarks
Q^{n}_{n}
can be higgsed simultaneously at small
\mu_{\rm x}\neq 0
.

45.5

m\gg\Lambda_{2}\,,\,\,2N_{c}-N_{F}=1

At
\mu_{\rm x}m\gg\Lambda_{2}^{2}
the behavior of all vs-vacua is the same and is
described in section 45.1. Therefore, we consider below only the case

\mu_{\rm x}\,m\ll\Lambda_{2}^{2}
when the vs-vacua with
2N_{c}-N_{F}=1
and
2N_{c}-N_{F}\geq 2

behave differently.

The pattern of flavor symmetry breaking remains fixed,

{\rm n}_{1}={\overline{N}}_{c}=N_{c}-1\,,\,\,{\rm n}_{2}=N_{c}\,,\,\,U(N_{F})\rightarrow U({\rm n}_{1})\times U({\rm n}_{2})
,
as well as the multiplicity

N_{vs}=C^{\,{\rm n}_{2}=N_{c}}_{N_{F}}=C^{\,{\rm n}_{1}={\overline{N}}_{c}}_{N_{F}}
. And
\langle a_{0}\rangle=m
also remains the same in all vs -vacua (really, at all

m\gtrless\Lambda_{2}
) and still ”eats” all quark masses
"m"
. But the
discrete symmetry
Z_{2N_{c}-N_{F}}
becomes trivial at
2N_{c}-N_{F}=1
and gives
no restrictions on the form of
\langle X^{\rm adj}_{SU(N_{c})}\rangle\sim\Lambda_{2}
(remind that
\langle X^{\rm adj}_{SU(N_{c})}\rangle\sim\Lambda_{2}
is higgsed necessarily at
\mu_{\rm x}m\ll\Lambda_{2}^{2}
to avoid
g^{2}(\mu<\Lambda_{2})<0
). As above in section 45.2, the
right flavor symmetry breaking implies the unbroken
SU({\overline{N}}_{c})
lower
energy group and a presence of
N_{F}
flavors of quark-like particles
massless at
\mu_{\rm x}\rightarrow 0
, then
{\rm n}_{1}={\overline{N}}_{c}
of them will be higgsed at
small
\mu_{\rm x}\neq 0
and there will remain
2{\overline{N}}_{c}N_{c}

Nambu-Goldstone multiplets.

However, it is not difficult to see that a literal picture with
light all BPS original electric particles
of remained unbroken at the
scale
\mu\sim\Lambda_{2}
electric subgroup
SU({\overline{N}}_{c})
cannot be right at

2N_{c}-N_{F}=1
. Indeed,
X^{\rm adj}_{SU(N_{c})}
is higgsed now as

\langle X^{\rm adj}_{SU(N_{c})}\rangle\sim\Lambda_{2}\,{\rm diag}(\,1...1\,;\,-{\overline{N}}_{c}\,),\,\,{\overline{N}}_{c}=N_{c}-1
. And all original quarks
Q^{i},\,{\overline{Q}}_{i}

with
SU(N_{c})
colors acquire masses
\sim\Lambda_{2}
and
decouple at
\mu\lesssim\Lambda_{2}
.

60
60

60

Clearly, at
m\ll\Lambda_{2},\,\mu_{\rm x}\rightarrow 0
and
2N_{c}-N_{F}=1
, this argument concerns
also
SU(N_{c})
br2 vacua with
0<{\rm n}_{1}<{\overline{N}}_{c}
and the multiplicity

({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})C^{{\rm n}_{1}}_{N_{F}}
, and S vacua with the multiplicity
{\overline{N}}_{c}
,
both with
\langle S\rangle_{N_{c}}\neq 0
and with the
SU({\overline{N}}_{c}=N_{c}-1)

lower energy gauge group at energy
\mu<\Lambda_{2}/(\rm several)
.

Therefore, the vs-vacua with
2N_{c}-N_{F}=1
are not typical vacua, they
are really exceptional. The absence of the non-trivial unbroken

Z_{2N_{c}-N_{F}}
symmetry is crucial.

61

61

Considered in
[
37
]
example of
U(N_{c})
vs-vacua (
r=N_{c}

vacua in the language of
[
37
]
) with
N_{c}=3,\,N_{F}=5,\,{\rm n}_{1}={\overline{N}}_{c}=N_{c}-1=2
and
\langle S\rangle_{N_{c}}=0
belongs just
to this exceptional type of vs-vacua with
2N_{c}-N_{F}=1
.

The possible exceptional properties of vacua with
2N_{c}-N_{F}=1
at
\mu_{\rm x}\rightarrow 0
are indicated also by the curve (

), its form is changed

:

[

.

For this reason we do not deal with such vacua in this paper.
