\mathbf{SU(N_{c}),\,\,m\gg\Lambda_{2},\,\,U(N_{F})\rightarrow U({\rm n}_{1})\times U({\rm n}_{2})},
br2 vacua

46.1

Larger
\mu_{\rm x}

The quark condensates in these br2 vacua with
{\rm n}_{1}\geq 1,\,{\rm n}_{2}<N_{c}
are obtained from (
43.1
),(
43.1.1
) by the
replacement
{\rm n}_{1}\leftrightarrow{\rm n}_{2}
, i.e. :

\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{N_{c}}\approx\mu_{\rm x}\hat{m}\gg\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{N_{c}},\,\hat{m}=m\,N_{c}/(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{2})
. The discrete
Z_{2N_{c}-N_{F}\geq 2}

symmetry is also unbroken in these vacua and
the multiplicity is
N_{\rm br2}=(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{2})C^{\,{\rm n}_{2}}_{N_{F}}
. They
evolve at
m\ll\Lambda_{2}
to Lt-vacua with
\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{N_{c}}\sim\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{N_{c}}\sim\mu_{\rm x}\Lambda_{2}
.

Similarly to br1 vacua, the scalars
X^{adj}
are also higgsed at the
largest scale
\mu\sim m\gg\Lambda_{2}
in the weak coupling region, now as

SU(N_{c})\rightarrow SU({\rm n}_{2})\times U(1)\times SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{2}),\,\,N_{F}/2<{\rm n}_{2}<N_{c}
,
so that (the leading terms only)

\displaystyle\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{N_{c}}\approx\mu_{\rm x}\hat{m}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{N_{c}}\approx\mu_{\rm x}\,\hat{m}\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{2}}{\hat{m}}\Bigr)^{\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{2}}}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\hat{m}=\frac{N_{c}}{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{2}}\,m\,,

(46.1.1)

\langle S\rangle_{N_{c}}=\frac{\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{N_{c}}\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{N_{c}}}{\mu_{\rm x}}\approx\mu_{\rm x}\,\hat{m}^{2}\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{2}}{\hat{m}}\Bigr)^{\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{2}}}\ll\mu_{\rm x}\,m^{2}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\frac{\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{N_{c}}}{\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{N_{c}}}\approx\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{2}}{\hat{m}}\Bigr)^{\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{2}}}\ll 1\,,

\displaystyle\langle X^{\rm adj}_{SU(N_{c})}\rangle\equiv\langle\,X^{adj}_{SU({\rm n}_{2})}+X_{U(1)}+X^{adj}_{SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{2})}\,\rangle\,,

(46.1.2)

\sqrt{2}\,X_{U(1)}=a\,{\rm diag}\Bigl(\underbrace{\,1}_{{\rm n}_{2}}\,;\,\underbrace{\,\widehat{c}}_{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{2}}\,\Bigr)\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\widehat{c}=-\frac{{\rm n}_{2}}{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{2}}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\langle a\rangle=m\,,

\mu_{\rm x}\langle{\rm Tr}\,(\sqrt{2}X^{\rm adj}_{SU(N_{c})})^{2}\rangle=(2N_{c}-N_{F})\langle S\rangle_{N_{c}}+m\langle{\rm Tr}\,(({\overline{Q}}Q))\rangle_{N_{c}}\approx m({\rm n}_{2}\mu_{\rm x}\hat{m})\,.

As a result, all quarks charged under
SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{2})
and hybrids

SU(N_{c})/[\,SU({\rm n}_{2})\times SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{2})\times U(1)\,]
acquire large
masses
\hat{m}=m-{\widehat{c}}\,\langle a\rangle=mN_{c}/(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{2})
and decouple
at scales
\mu<\,\hat{m}
, there remains
{\cal N}=2\,\,SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{2})

SYM with the scale factor
\Lambda^{SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{2})}_{{\cal N}=2\,SYM}
of its gauge coupling, see also (
46.1.1
),

\displaystyle\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{2})}_{{\cal N}=2\,SYM}\rangle^{2}=\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{2}^{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{(\hat{m})}^{N_{F}}}{{(\hat{m})}^{2{\rm n}_{2}}}\Bigr)^{\frac{1}{(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{2})}}={\hat{m}^{2}}\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{2}}{{\hat{m}}}\Bigr)^{\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{2})}}\ll\Lambda_{2}^{2}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\hat{m}=\frac{N_{c}}{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{2}}m\,,

(46.1.3)

\langle S\rangle_{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{2}}=\mu_{\rm x}\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{2})}_{{\cal N}=2\,SYM}\rangle^{2}\approx\langle S\rangle_{N_{c}}\,.

At
\mu_{\rm x}\ll\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{2})}_{{\cal N}=2\,SYM}\rangle
it
is higgsed in a standard way
[
31
]
,
SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{2})\rightarrow U^{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{2}-1}(1)
, due to higgsing of
X^{adj}_{SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{2})}

\displaystyle\langle{\sqrt{2}}X^{adj}_{SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{2})}\rangle\sim\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{2})}_{{\cal N}=2\,SYM}\rangle\,{\rm diag}\Bigl(\underbrace{0}_{{\rm n}_{2}}\,;\,\underbrace{k_{1},\,...\,k_{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{2}}}_{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{2}}\Bigr)\,,\hskip 11.74988ptk_{i}=O(1)\,.

(46.1.4)

Note that the value
\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c})}_{{\cal N}=2\,SYM}\rangle
of
\langle X^{adj}_{SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{2})}\rangle
in
(
46.1.3
),(
46.1.4
) is consistent with the unbroken

Z_{2N_{c}-N_{F}}
discrete symmetry.

As a result,
\,N_{c}-{\rm n}_{2}-1
pure magnetic monopoles
M_{\rm n}

(massless at
\mu_{\rm x}\rightarrow 0
) with the
SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{2})
adjoint charges are
formed at the scale
\mu\sim\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{2})}_{{\cal N}=2\,SYM}\rangle
in this SYM sector. These correspond to
N_{c}-{\rm n}_{2}-1

unequal double roots of the curve (
40.2
) connected with this
SYM sector. Note also that two single roots with
(e^{+}-e^{-})\sim\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c})}_{{\cal N}=2\,SYM}\rangle
of the curve
(
40.2
) also originate from this SYM sector. Other
{\rm n}_{2}
double
roots of the curve at
\mu_{\rm x}\rightarrow 0
originate in these vacua from the

SU({\rm n}_{2})
sector, see below.

These
N_{c}-{\rm n}_{2}-1
monopoles are all higgsed with
\langle M_{\rm n}\rangle=\langle{\overline{M}}_{\rm n}\rangle\sim\,(\mu_{\rm x}\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{2})}_{{\cal N}=2\,SYM}\rangle)^{1/2}
, so   that

N_{c}-{\rm n}_{2}-1
long
{\cal N}=2
multiplets of massive photons
appear, with masses
\sim(\mu_{\rm x}\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{2})}_{{\cal N}=2\,SYM}\rangle)^{1/2}
. Besides, this leads to a weak confinement
of all heavier original
SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{2})
electrically charged particles,
i.e. quarks and all hybrids with masses
\sim m
, and all
{\cal N}=2\,\,SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{2})
SYM adjoint charged particles with masses

\sim\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{2})}_{{\cal N}=2\,SYM}\rangle
,
the tension of the confining string is
\sigma^{1/2}_{2}\sim(\mu_{\rm x}\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{2})}_{{\cal N}=2\,SYM}\rangle)^{1/2}\ll\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{2})}_{{\cal N}=2\,SYM}\rangle\ll\Lambda_{2}\ll m
. The factor

N_{c}-{\rm n}_{2}
in the multiplicity of these br2 vacua arises just from
this
{\cal N}=2\,\,SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{2})
SYM part.

At larger
\mu_{\rm x}
in the range
\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{2})}_{{\cal N}=2\,SYM}\rangle\ll\mu_{\rm x}\ll m
, the scalars
X^{adj}_{SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{2})}

become too heavy, their light
SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{2})
physical phases fluctuate
then freely at the scale
\sim\mu_{\rm x}^{\rm pole}=g^{2}(\mu=\mu_{\rm x}^{\rm pole})\mu_{\rm x}
and they are not higgsed. Instead, they decouple as heavy
in the weak coupling region at scales
\mu<\mu_{\rm x}^{\rm pole}/(\rm several)
. There remains then
{\cal N}=1\,\,SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{2})
SYM with
the scale factor
\Lambda^{SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{2})}_{{\cal N}=1\,SYM}
of its
gauge coupling

\displaystyle\langle S\rangle_{{\cal N}=1\,SYM}=\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{2})}_{{\cal N}=1\,SYM}\rangle^{3}=\mu_{\rm x}\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{2})}_{{\cal N}=2\,SYM}\rangle^{2},\,\,\,\,\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{2})}_{{\cal N}=2\,SYM}\rangle\ll\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{2})}_{{\cal N}=1\,SYM}\rangle\ll\mu_{\rm x}\ll m\,.\hskip 11.74988pt

(46.1.5)

Therefore, there will be in this case a large number of strongly
coupled
SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{2})
gluonia with the mass scale
\sim\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{2})}_{{\cal N}=1\,SYM}\rangle
, while
all original heavier charged particles with
SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{2})

electric charges and masses either
\sim m\gg\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{2})}_{{\cal N}=1\,SYM}\rangle

or
\sim g^{2}\mu_{\rm x}\gg\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{2})}_{{\cal N}=1\,SYM}\rangle
still will be weakly confined, the tension of the
confining string is larger in this case,

\sigma^{1/2}_{1}\sim\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{2})}_{{\cal N}=1\,SYM}\rangle
. This
{\cal N}=1\,\,SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{2})\,
SYM gives the
same factor
N_{c}-{\rm n}_{2}
in the multiplicity of these vacua.

Now, about the electric
SU({\rm n}_{2})
part decoupled from

SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{2})
SYM. The scale factor of its gauge coupling is

\displaystyle\langle\Lambda_{SU({\rm n}_{2})}\rangle=\Bigl(\,\frac{\Lambda_{2}^{2N_{c}-N_{F}}}{(\hat{m})^{2(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{2})}}\,\Bigr)^{\frac{1}{2{\rm n}_{2}-N_{F}}}=\hat{m}\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{2}}{\hat{m}}\Bigr)^{\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{2{\rm n}_{2}-N_{F}}}\ll\Lambda_{2}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\hat{m}=\frac{N_{c}}{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{2}}m\,.

(46.1.6)

Note that the value of
\langle\Lambda_{SU({\rm n}_{2})}\rangle
in
(
46.1.6
) is also consistent with the unbroken
Z_{2N_{c}-N_{F}}

discrete symmetry.

What
qualitatively differs
this
SU({\rm n}_{2})
part in br2 vacua at
scales
\mu<\,m
from its analog
SU({\rm n}_{1})
in br1 vacua of section
43.1 is that
SU({\rm n}_{1})
with
N_{F}>2{\rm n}_{1}
is IR free, while
SU({\rm n}_{2})

with
N_{F}<2{\rm n}_{2}
is UV free, and its small
{\cal N}=2
gauge coupling
at the scale
\mu\sim m\gg\Lambda_{2}
begins to grow logarithmically with
diminished energy at
\mu<m
. Therefore,
if nothing prevents
,

\,\,X^{adj}_{SU({\rm n}_{2})}
will be higgsed necessarily at the scale

\sim\langle\Lambda_{SU({\rm n}_{2})}\rangle
, with
\langle X^{adj}_{SU({\rm n}_{2})}\rangle\sim\langle\Lambda_{SU({\rm n}_{2})}\rangle
, to
avoid   unphysical
g^{2}(\mu<\langle\Lambda_{SU({\rm n}_{2})}\rangle)<0

(see section 46.2 below).

But if
(\mu_{\rm x}m)^{1/2}\gg\langle\Lambda_{SU({\rm n}_{2})}\rangle
,
the
phase will be different
. The leading effect in this case will be the
breaking of the whole
SU({\rm n}_{2})
group due to higgsing of
{\rm n}_{2}<N_{c}

out   of
N_{F}
quarks
Q^{i},\,{\overline{Q}}_{i}
in the weak coupling region,
with e.g.
\langle Q^{i=b+{\rm n}_{1}}_{b}\rangle\sim\delta^{i}_{b}(\mu_{\rm x}m)^{1/2},\,\,b=1...{\rm n}_{2},\,\,i={\rm n}_{1}+1...N_{F}
. This will give the
additional factor
C^{\,{\rm n}_{2}}_{N_{F}}

in the multiplicity of these br2 vacua due to spontaneous
flavor symmetry breaking
U(N_{F})\rightarrow U({\rm n}_{1})\times U({\rm n}_{2})
,
so that the overall multiplicity will be
(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{2})C^{\,{\rm n}_{2}}_{N_{F}}
,
as it should be. The corresponding parts of the superpotential will be
as in (
43.1.3
),(
43.1.4
), with a replacement
{\rm n}_{1}\rightarrow{\rm n}_{2}
,

\displaystyle{\cal W}_{{\rm n}_{2}}=(\,m-a\,)\,{\rm Tr}\,({\overline{Q}}Q)_{{\rm n}_{2}}-{\rm Tr}\,({\overline{Q}}\sqrt{2}X^{\rm adj}_{SU({\rm n}_{2})}Q)_{{\rm n}_{2}}+\mu_{\rm x}{\rm Tr}\,(X^{\rm adj}_{SU({\rm n}_{2})})^{2}+\frac{\mu_{\rm x}}{2}\,\frac{{\rm n}_{2}N_{c}}{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{2}}\,a^{2}\,.

(46.1.7)

From (
46.1.7
) in the case considered (no summation
over
j
in (
46.1.8
) )

\langle a\rangle=m,\,\,\langle X^{adj}_{SU({\rm n}_{2})}\rangle=0,\,\,\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{{\rm n}_{2}}=\sum_{b=1}^{{\rm n}_{2}}\langle{\overline{Q}}_{2}^{\,b}Q^{2}_{b}\rangle=\langle{\overline{Q}}_{2}^{\,2}\rangle\langle Q^{2}_{2}\rangle\approx\frac{N_{c}}{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{2}}\mu_{\rm x}\,m=\mu_{\rm x}\,\hat{m}\,,

\displaystyle\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{{\rm n}_{2}}=\sum_{b=1}^{{\rm n}_{2}}\langle{\overline{Q}}_{j}^{\,b}\rangle\langle Q^{j}_{b}\rangle=0\,,\,\,j=1...{\rm n}_{1},\,\,\langle{S}\rangle_{{\rm n}_{2}}=\frac{1}{\mu_{\rm x}}\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{{\rm n}_{2}}\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{{\rm n}_{2}}=0\,.\hskip 11.74988pt

(46.1.8)

2{\rm n}_{1}{\rm n}_{2}
massless Nambu-Goldstone multiplets are formed as a result
of the spontaneous flavor symmetry breaking,
\Pi_{j}^{i}=({\overline{Q}}_{j}Q^{i})_{{\rm n}_{2}}\,\rightarrow\,({\overline{Q}}_{j}\langle Q^{i}\rangle)_{{\rm n}_{2}},\,\,\Pi_{i}^{j}=({\overline{Q}}_{i}Q^{j})_{{\rm n}_{2}}\,\rightarrow\,(\langle{\overline{Q}}_{i}\rangle Q^{j})_{{\rm n}_{2}},\,\,i={\rm n}_{1}+1...N_{F},\,\,j=1...{\rm n}_{1},\,\,\langle\Pi^{i}_{j}\rangle=\langle\Pi^{j}_{i}\rangle=0
(in essence, these
are   not higgsed quarks with
{\rm n}_{1}
flavors and
SU({\rm n}_{2})
colors).
Besides, there are
{\rm n}_{2}^{2}
long
{\cal N}=2
multiplets of massive
gluons with masses
\sim(\mu_{\rm x}m)^{1/2}
.

We emphasize that higgsing of
{\rm n}_{2}
quarks at the higher scale

(\mu_{\rm x}\,m)^{1/2}\gg\langle\Lambda_{SU({\rm n}_{2})}\rangle
, see (
46.1.7
), (
46.1.8
),
prevents

X^{adj}_{SU({\rm n}_{2})}
from higgsing
, i.e.
\langle X^{adj}_{SU({\rm n}_{2})}\rangle_{{\rm n}_{2}}
is not simply smaller, but
exactly zero (see also section 45.1 for a similar regime).

On the whole for these br2 vacua in the case considered, i.e. at

(m\mu_{\rm x})^{1/2}\gg\langle\Lambda_{SU({\rm n}_{2})}\rangle
, all qualitative properties are similar to those of br1
vacua   in section   43.1.

46.2

Smaller
\mu_{\rm x}

Consider now the behavior of the
SU({\rm n}_{2})\times U(1)

part in the opposite case of smaller

\mu_{\rm x}\ll\langle\Lambda_{SU({\rm n}_{2})}\rangle^{2}/m,\,\langle\Lambda_{SU({\rm n}_{2})}\rangle\ll\Lambda_{2}\ll m
. As will be seen below, in
this case even all qualitative properties of mass spectra in this part
will be quite different (the behavior of the
SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{2})
SYM part
decoupled from
SU({\rm n}_{2})\times U(1)
was described above in section 46.1).

Now, at smaller
\mu_{\rm x}
, instead of quarks, the scalar field

X^{adj}_{SU({\rm n}_{2})}
is higgsed first at the scale

\sim\langle\Lambda_{SU({\rm n}_{2})}\rangle
to avoid unphysical

of UV free
{\cal N}=2\,\,SU({\rm n}_{2})

respects
the original

discrete symmetry of the UV free
SU(N_{c})

theory, see (
46.1.6
), because the
SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{2})
SYM part is
decoupled, there is now the analog of
Z_{2N_{c}-N_{F}}
in the
SU({\rm n}_{2})

group   with
N_{F}
flavors of quarks, this is the unbroken non-trivial

Z_{2{\rm n}_{2}-N_{F}}=Z_{{\rm n}_{2}-{\rm n}_{1}}
discrete symmetry with
{\rm n}_{2}-{\rm n}_{1}\geq 2
,

{\rm b}_{2}=(2N_{c}-N_{F})\rightarrow{\rm b}^{\prime}_{2}=(2{\rm n}_{2}-N_{F})=({\rm n}_{2}-{\rm n}_{1})
.
Therefore, the field
X^{adj}_{SU({\rm n}_{2})}
is higgsed at
{\rm n}_{2}-{\rm n}_{1}\geq 2

at the scale
\mu\sim\langle\Lambda_{SU({\rm n}_{2})}\rangle
(in the strong
coupling and nonperturbative regime), qualitatively similarly to
(
41.1.1
),(
41.1.2
) in section 41.1 :
SU({\rm n}_{2})\rightarrow SU({\rm n}_{1})\times U^{(1)}(1)\times U^{{\rm n}_{2}-{\rm n}_{1}-1}(1)
. There will be similar dyons
D_{j}

(massless at
\mu_{\rm x}\rightarrow 0
) etc., and a whole qualitative picture will be
similar to   those in section 41.1, with evident replacements of
parameters. The only qualitative difference is that the additional SYM
part is absent here because
{\rm n}_{1}
quark flavors are higgsed now in

SU({\rm n}_{1})
, see (
41.1.1
),(
41.1.16
),

\displaystyle\langle X^{adj}_{SU({\rm n}_{2})}\rangle=\langle X^{adj}_{SU({\rm n}_{1})}+X^{(1)}_{U(1)}+X^{adj}_{SU({\rm n}_{2}-{\rm n}_{1})}\rangle,

(46.2.1)

\langle\sqrt{2}X^{adj}_{SU({\rm n}_{2}-{\rm n}_{1})}\rangle=C_{{\rm n}_{2}-{\rm n}_{1}}\langle\Lambda_{SU({\rm n}_{2})}\rangle\,{\rm\,diag}\,\Bigl(\,\underbrace{0}_{{\rm n}_{1}}\,;\,\underbrace{\,\tau^{0},\,\tau^{1},\,...,\,\tau^{{\rm n}_{2}-{\rm n}_{1}-1}}_{{\rm n}_{2}-{\rm n}_{1}}\,;\underbrace{0}_{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{2}}\,\Bigr)\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\tau=\exp\{\frac{2\pi i}{{\rm n}_{2}-{\rm n}_{1}}\}\,,

\sqrt{2}\,X^{(1)}_{U(1)}=a_{1}\,{\rm diag}\,(\,\underbrace{\,1}_{{\rm n}_{1}}\,;\,\underbrace{\,\widehat{c}_{1}}_{{\rm n}_{2}-{\rm n}_{1}}\,;\underbrace{0}_{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{2}}\,),\hskip 11.74988pt\widehat{c}_{1}=-\,\frac{{\rm n}_{1}}{{\rm n}_{2}-{\rm n}_{1}}\,.\hskip 11.74988pt

Therefore, similarly to (
41.1.2
),(
45.2.2
), in this case
the low energy superpotential of this

sector
can be written as

62
62

62

In other words, in this case the
SU(N^{\prime}_{c}={\rm n}_{2})\times U(1)
part
of

with

and with decoupled

of section 45.2, with

of

in (

\displaystyle{\mathcal{W}}_{{\rm n}_{2}}={\mathcal{W}}_{{\rm n}_{1}}+{\mathcal{W}}_{D}+{\mathcal{W}}_{a,\,a_{1}}+\dots\,,

(46.2.2)

{\mathcal{W}}_{{\rm n}_{1}}=(m-a-a_{1}){\rm Tr}\,({\overline{Q}}Q)_{{\rm n}_{1}}-{\rm Tr}\,\Bigl({\overline{Q}}\sqrt{2}X_{SU({\rm n}_{1})}^{\rm adj}Q\Bigr)_{{\rm n}_{1}}+\mu_{\rm x}(1+\widehat{\delta}_{2}){\rm Tr}\,(X^{\rm adj}_{SU({\rm n}_{1})})^{2}\,,

{\mathcal{W}}_{D}=\Bigl(m-a-\widehat{c}_{1}a_{1}\Bigr)\sum_{j=1}^{{\rm n}_{2}-{\rm n}_{1}}{\overline{D}}_{j}D_{j}-\sum_{j=1}^{{\rm n}_{2}-{\rm n}_{1}}a_{D,j}{\overline{D}}_{j}D_{j}-\mu_{\rm x}\Lambda_{SU({\rm n}_{2})}\sum_{j=1}^{{\rm n}_{2}-{\rm n}_{1}}\tau^{j-1}a_{D,j}+\mu_{\rm x}{\widehat{L}}\,\Bigl(\sum_{j=1}^{{\rm n}_{2}-{\rm n}_{1}}a_{D,j}\Bigr),

{\mathcal{W}}_{a,\,a_{1}}=\frac{\mu_{\rm x}}{2}\frac{{\rm n}_{2}N_{c}}{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{2}}\,a^{2}+\frac{\mu_{\rm x}}{2}\frac{{\rm n}_{1}{\rm n}_{2}}{{\rm n}_{2}-{\rm n}_{1}}(1+\widehat{\delta}_{1})\,a^{2}_{1}+\mu_{\rm x}{\rm n}_{2}\widehat{\delta}_{3}a_{1}(m-a-\widehat{c}_{1}a_{1})+\mu_{\rm x}{\rm n}_{2}\widehat{\delta}_{4}\,(m-a-\widehat{c}_{1}a_{1})^{2}\,,

where
\widehat{L}
is the Lagrange multiplier field,

\langle\widehat{L}\rangle=O(m)
and dots denote small power corrections.
The additional terms with
\widehat{\delta}_{1,2,3,4}
, as previously in
br2 vacua in section 41.1 and in vs -vacua in section 45.2, originate
from integrating out all heavier fields with masses

\sim\Lambda_{SU({\rm n}_{2})}
in the soft background. Here these soft

(m-a-\widehat{c}_{1}a_{1})
and
[(1-\widehat{c}_{1})a_{1}+\sqrt{2}X^{\rm adj}_{SU({\rm n}_{1})}]
( while

of

in (
41.1.1
) ). These constants
\widehat{\delta}_{i}
differ
from (

) only because the number of colors is different,

, i.e.
\widehat{\delta}_{1}=[\,-2N^{\prime}_{c}/(2N^{\prime}_{c}-N_{F})]=[\,-2{\rm n}_{2}/({\rm n}_{2}-{\rm n}_{1})\,],\,\,\widehat{\delta}_{2}=-2
(while
\widehat{\delta}_{3}=0
as in br2 vacua, see (
47.4
)).

The charges of fields and parameters entering (
46.2.2
)
under
Z_{{\rm n}_{2}-{\rm n}_{1}}=\exp\{i\pi/({\rm n}_{2}-{\rm n}_{1})\}
transformation are :

q_{\lambda}=q_{\rm\theta}=1,\,\,q_{X_{SU({\rm n}_{1})}^{\rm adj}}=q_{a}=q_{a_{1}}=q_{a_{D,j}}=q_{\rm m}=q_{\widehat{L}}=2,\,\,q_{Q}=q_{\overline{Q}}=q_{D_{j}}=q_{{\overline{D}}_{j}}=q_{\Lambda_{SU({\rm n}_{2})}}=0,\,\,q_{\mu_{\rm x}}=-2
. The non-trivial

of dual scalars

and dyons in (

is

-invariant.

From (
46.2.2
):

\displaystyle\langle a\rangle=m\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\langle a_{1}\rangle=0\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\langle a_{D,j}\rangle=0\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\langle X_{SU({\rm n}_{1})}^{\rm adj}\rangle=0\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\langle S\rangle_{{\rm n}_{1}}=0\,,

(46.2.3)

\langle{\overline{D}}_{j}D_{j}\rangle=\langle{\overline{D}}_{j}\rangle\langle D_{j}\rangle\approx-\mu_{\rm x}\langle\Lambda_{SU({\rm n}_{2})}\rangle\tau^{j-1}+\mu_{\rm x}\langle{\widehat{L}}\rangle\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\langle\Sigma_{D}\rangle=\sum_{j=1}^{{\rm n}_{2}-{\rm n}_{1}}\langle{\overline{D}}_{j}\rangle\langle D_{j}\rangle=({\rm n}_{2}-{\rm n}_{1})\mu_{\rm x}\langle{\widehat{L}}\rangle,

\langle{\widehat{L}}\rangle\approx\frac{N_{c}}{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{2}}\,m=\hat{m}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\langle{\rm Tr}\,({\overline{Q}}Q)\rangle_{{\rm n}_{1}}={\rm n}_{1}\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{{\rm n}_{1}}+{\rm n}_{2}\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{{\rm n}_{1}}\approx{\rm n}_{1}\mu_{\rm x}\,\hat{m}\,,

\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{{\rm n}_{1}}=\langle{\overline{Q}}^{\,1}_{1}\rangle\langle Q^{1}_{1}\rangle\approx\frac{N_{c}}{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{2}}\,\mu_{\rm x}m\approx\mu_{\rm x}\hat{m}\approx\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{N_{c}}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{{\rm n}_{1}}=\sum_{a=1}^{{\rm n}_{1}}\langle{\overline{Q}}^{\,a}_{2}\rangle\langle Q^{2}_{a}\rangle=0\,.

The multiplicity of these br2 vacua in the case considered is
N_{\rm br2}=(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{2})C^{\,{\rm n}_{1}}_{N_{F}}=(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{2})C^{\,{\rm n}_{2}}_{N_{F}}
, as it should
be. The factor
(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{2})
originates from the
SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{2})
SYM. The
factor
C^{\,{\rm n}_{1}}_{N_{F}}
is finally a consequence of the color
breaking
SU({\rm n}_{2})\rightarrow SU({\rm n}_{1})\times U(1)^{{\rm n}_{2}-{\rm n}_{1}}
with the unbroken

Z_{2{\rm n}_{2}-N_{F}}=Z_{{\rm n}_{2}-{\rm n}_{1}}
discrete symmetry, and spontaneous breaking
of flavor symmetry due to higgsing of
{\rm n}_{1}
quarks flavors (massless
at
\mu_{\rm x}\rightarrow 0
) in the
SU({\rm n}_{1})
color subgroup of
SU({\rm n}_{2})
,
\langle Q^{i}_{a}\rangle=\langle{\overline{Q}}^{\,a}_{i}\rangle\approx\delta_{a}^{i}(\mu_{\rm x}\hat{m})^{1/2}
,
a=1...{\rm n}_{1},\,\,i=1...N_{F}
.

Note also that if the non-trivial discrete symmetry
Z_{{\rm n}_{2}-{\rm n}_{1}\geq 2}
were broken spontaneously in the
SU({\rm n}_{2})
subgroup at the scale

\mu\sim\langle\Lambda_{SU({\rm n}_{2})}\rangle\gg(\mu_{\rm x}m)^{1/2}
, this
would lead then
to the factor
{\rm n}_{2}-{\rm n}_{1}
in the multiplicity of these vacua, and this
is a wrong factor.

Due to higgsing of
{\rm n}_{2}-{\rm n}_{1}
dyons with the nonzero
SU({\rm n}_{2}-{\rm n}_{1})

adjoint magnetic charges,
\langle{\overline{D}_{j}}\rangle\langle D_{j}\rangle\sim\mu_{\rm x}m\gg\mu_{\rm x}\Lambda_{2}\gg\mu_{\rm x}\langle\Lambda_{SU({\rm n}_{2})}\rangle
,
all original pure electrically charged particles with
SU({\rm n}_{2}-{\rm n}_{1})

colors and masses either
\sim m
or

\sim\langle\Lambda_{SU({\rm n}_{2})}\rangle
are weakly confined, the
string tension is
\sigma^{1/2}\sim(\mu_{\rm x}m)^{1/2}\ll\langle\Lambda_{SU({\rm n}_{2})}\rangle\ll\Lambda_{2}\ll m
. Besides,

{\rm n}_{2}-{\rm n}_{1}
long
{\cal N}=2
multiplets of massive photons with masses

\sim(\mu_{\rm x}m)^{1/2}
are formed. Remind that, due to higgsing of

N_{c}-{\rm n}_{2}-1
magnetic monopoles
M_{\rm n}
from
SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{2})

SYM at
\mu\sim(\mu_{\rm x}\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{2})}_{{\cal N}=2\,SYM}\rangle)^{1/2}
, all original pure electrically charged particles
with
SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{2})
charges and masses either
\sim m
or

\sim\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{2})}_{{\cal N}=2\,SYM}\rangle
are also
weakly confined, but this string tension
\sigma^{1/2}_{2}\sim(\mu_{\rm x}\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{2})}_{{\cal N}=2\,SYM}\rangle)^{1/2}\ll(\mu_{\rm x}m)^{1/2}
is much smaller.

Due to higgsing of
{\rm n}_{1}
flavors of original electric quarks in the IR
free color
SU({\rm n}_{1})
with
N_{F}>2{\rm n}_{1}
flavors, there are
{\rm n}_{1}^{2}
long

{\cal N}=2
multiplets of massive gluons with masses
\sim(\mu_{\rm x}m)^{1/2}\ll\langle\Lambda_{SU({\rm n}_{2})}\rangle
, while the original
non-higgsed quarks with
{\rm n}_{2}
flavors and
SU({\rm n}_{1})
colors form

2{\rm n}_{1}{\rm n}_{2}
massless Nambu-Goldstone multiplets. There are no other
massless particles at
\mu_{\rm x}\neq 0
. And there are no particles with
masses
\sim m\gg\Lambda_{2}
in this
SU({\rm n}_{2})\times U(1)
sector.

Remind that the curve (
40.2
) has
N_{c}-1
double roots in these
br2 vacua at
\mu_{\rm x}\rightarrow 0
. Of them:
{\rm n}_{2}-{\rm n}_{1}
unequal roots correspond
to
{\rm n}_{2}-{\rm n}_{1}
dyons
D_{j},\,\,{\rm n}_{1}
equal roots correspond to
{\rm n}_{1}

higgsed quarks
Q^{i}
of
SU({\rm n}_{1})
, the remaining unequal
N_{c}-{\rm n}_{2}-1

double roots correspond to
N_{c}-{\rm n}_{2}-1
pure magnetic monopoles

M_{\rm n}
from
SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{2})\,\,{\cal N}=2
SYM. Two single
roots with
(e^{+}-e^{-})\sim\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{2})}_{{\cal N}=2\,SYM}\rangle

originate from this
{\cal N}=2
SYM.

Besides, similarly to vs-vacua in section 45, there is a phase
transition in these br2 vacua in the region

\mu_{\rm x}\sim\langle\Lambda_{SU({\rm n}_{2})}\rangle^{2}/m
. E.g., all
SU({\rm n}_{2}-{\rm n}_{1})

charged gluons have equal masses in the whole wide range
(\rm several)\langle\Lambda_{SU({\rm n}_{2})}\rangle<(\mu_{\rm x}m)^{1/2}\ll m
of the
higher energy phase of section 46.1, while masses of these gluons
differ greatly at
\mu_{\rm x}\rightarrow 0
in the lower energy phase of this
section,
\mu^{ij}_{gl}\sim|(\tau^{i}-\tau^{j})\langle\Lambda_{SU({\rm n}_{2})}\rangle|,\,i\neq j,\,i,j=0...{\rm n}_{2}-{\rm n}_{1}-1
. And
\langle X^{adj}_{SU({\rm n}_{2}-{\rm n}_{1})}\rangle
,

\,\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{{\rm n}_{2}}
also behave non-analytically at

\mu_{\rm x}\gtrless\langle\Lambda_{SU({\rm n}_{2})}\rangle^{2}/m
. But, unlike the
phase transition at
\mu_{\rm x}\sim\Lambda_{2}^{2}/m
in
vs-vacua of section 45, the phase transition in these br2 vacua is
at much smaller
\mu_{\rm x}\sim\langle\Lambda_{SU({\rm n}_{2})}\rangle^{2}/m\,,\,\,\langle\Lambda_{SU({\rm n}_{2})}\rangle\ll\Lambda_{2}
, see   (
46.1.6
).

And finally, remind that the mass spectra in these br2 vacua with

{\rm n}_{2}<N_{c}
and
m\gg\Lambda_{2}
depend essentially on the value of
m/\Lambda_{2}
,
and all these vacua evolve at
m\ll\Lambda_{2}
to Lt-vacua with the
spontaneously broken discrete
Z_{2N_{c}-N_{F}}
symmetry, see
section 4 in
[
17
]
.

47

Large quark masses
\mathbf{m\gg\Lambda_{2},\,\,\,SU(N_{c}),\,\,\,1\leq N_{F}<N_{c}}

The vacua with the unbroken
U(N_{F})
flavor symmetry
(here and in what follows at not too small
N_{c}
and
N_{F}
) are in
this case the SYM-vacua with the multiplicity
N_{c}

, see section 3
in
[

]
. The vacua with the broken

(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{i})C^{\,{\rm n}_{i}}_{N_{F}},\,i=1,\,2
.

Except for S-vacua, all formulae for this case
N_{F}<N_{c},\,\,m\gg\Lambda_{2}

are the same as described above for br1 vacua in section 43, for SYM
vacua in section 44 and for br2 vacua in section 46 (and only
N_{F}<N_{c}

now). Therefore, we describe below only the mass spectra in S-vacua
with
m\gg\Lambda_{2},\,\mu_{\rm x}\ll\Lambda_{2}\,
.

From

{\mathcal{W}}^{\,\rm eff}_{\rm tot}(\Pi)=m\,{\rm Tr}\,({\overline{Q}}Q)_{N_{c}}-\frac{1}{2\mu_{\rm x}}\Biggl[\,\sum_{i,j=1}^{N_{F}}({\overline{Q}}_{j}Q^{i})_{N_{c}}({\overline{Q}}_{\,i}Q^{j})_{N_{c}}-\frac{1}{N_{c}}\Bigl({\rm Tr}\,({\overline{Q}}Q)_{N_{c}}\Bigr)^{2}\Biggr]+(N_{c}-N_{F})S_{N_{c}}\,,

S_{N_{c}}=\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{2}^{2N_{c}-N_{F}}\mu_{\rm x}^{N_{c}}}{\det(({\overline{Q}}Q))_{N_{c}}}\Bigr)^{\frac{1}{N_{c}-N_{F}}}\,,

the quark and gluino condensates look in these
SU(N_{c})
S-vacua
with
{\rm n}_{1}=0,\,{\rm n}_{2}=N_{F}
as,

\displaystyle\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)\rangle_{N_{c}}\approx\mu_{\rm x}\,\hat{m}\Bigl[1-\frac{N_{c}}{N_{c}-N_{F}}\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{2}}{\hat{m}}\Bigr)^{\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{N_{c}-N_{F}}}\,\Bigr],\,\,\hat{m}=\frac{N_{c}}{N_{c}-N_{F}}\,m,\,\,\,\langle S\rangle_{N_{c}}\approx\mu_{\rm x}\,\hat{m}^{2}\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{2}}{\hat{m}}\Bigr)^{\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{N_{c}-N_{F}}}.\,\,\,

(47.1)

It is seen from (
47.1
) that the non-trivial discrete symmetry

Z_{2N_{c}-N_{F}\geq 2}
is not broken.

The scalars

\sim m\gg\Lambda_{2}

in the weak coupling regime
,

, see (

\displaystyle\langle X^{adj}_{SU(N_{c})}\rangle=\langle X^{adj}_{SU(N_{F})}+X_{U(1)}+X^{adj}_{SU(N_{c}-N_{F})}\rangle,

(47.2)

\sqrt{2}\,X_{U(1)}=a\,{\rm diag}\,(\,\underbrace{\,1}_{N_{F}}\,;\,\underbrace{\,\widehat{c}}_{N_{c}-N_{F}}\,),\hskip 11.74988pt\widehat{c}=-\,\frac{N_{F}}{N_{c}-N_{F}}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\langle a\rangle=m\,.

The quarks in the
SU(N_{c}-N_{F})
sector and

.

After they are integrated out, the scale factor of the

),

\displaystyle\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c}-N_{F})}_{{\cal N}=2\,SYM}\rangle^{2}=\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{2}^{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{(\hat{m})}^{N_{F}}}{(\hat{m})^{2N_{F}}}\Bigr)^{\frac{1}{N_{c}-N_{F}}}={\hat{m}^{2}}\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{2}}{{\hat{m}}}\Bigr)^{\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{N_{c}-N_{F}}}\ll\Lambda_{2}^{2}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\hat{m}=\frac{N_{c}}{N_{c}-N_{F}}m\,,

(47.3)

\langle S\rangle_{N_{c}-N_{F}}=\mu_{\rm x}\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c}-N_{F})}_{{\cal N}=2\,SYM}\rangle^{2}=\langle S\rangle_{N_{c}}\,,

while those of
SU(N_{F})
SQCD is

\displaystyle\langle\Lambda_{SU(N_{F})}\rangle=\Bigl(\,\frac{\Lambda_{2}^{2N_{c}-N_{F}}}{(\hat{m})^{2(N_{c}-N_{F})}}\,\Bigr)^{\frac{1}{N_{F}}}=\hat{m}\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{2}}{\hat{m}}\Bigr)^{\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{N_{F}}}\ll\Lambda_{2}\,.

(47.4)

If
\mu_{\rm x}\ll\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c}-N_{F})}_{{\cal N}=2\,SYM}\rangle
, the
scalars

of

giving

\,{\cal N}=2

dual massive photons with masses

All heavy original particles with masses
\sim m

\sim\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c}-N_{F})}_{{\cal N}=2\,SYM}\rangle
are

.

On the other hand, if e.g.
\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c}-N_{F})}_{{\cal N}=2\,SYM}\rangle\ll\mu_{\rm x}\ll m
, see (

strongly coupled

. All

\sim m

.

Now, as for the
SU(N_{F})\times U(1)
sector. At the scale
\mu=m/(\rm several)
this is the UV free

),

[(N_{F}^{2}-1)+1]

of

\displaystyle\langle\sqrt{2}X^{adj}_{SU(N_{F})}\rangle=C_{N_{F}}\langle\Lambda_{SU(N_{F})}\rangle\,{\rm diag}\,\Bigl(\,\underbrace{{\tilde{\rho}}^{\,0},\,{\tilde{\rho}}^{\,1},\,...,\,{\tilde{\rho}}^{\,N_{F}-1}}_{N_{F}}\,;\underbrace{0}_{N_{c}-N_{F}}\,\Bigr),\hskip 11.74988pt\tilde{\rho}=\exp\{\,\frac{2\pi i}{N_{F}}\,\}\,,

\approx\,\mu_{\rm x}\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c}-N_{F})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle^{2}\Biggl[(N_{c}-N_{F})-N_{F}\frac{(2N_{c}-N_{F})}{N_{c}-N_{F}}\,\frac{\hat{a}}{\hat{m}}\,\Biggr]\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\hat{m}=\frac{N_{c}}{N_{c}-N_{F}}m\,,\hskip 11.74988pta=\langle a\rangle+{\hat{a}}\,.

From (

)

\langle a\rangle=m\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\langle a_{D,\,j}\rangle=0\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\langle{\overline{D}}_{j}\rangle\langle D_{j}\rangle=-\mu_{\rm x}\langle\Lambda_{SU(N_{F})}\rangle{\tilde{\rho}}^{\,{j-1}}+\mu_{\rm x}\langle{\tilde{L}}\rangle\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\langle\Sigma_{D}\rangle=\sum_{j=1}^{N_{F}}\langle{\overline{D}}_{j}\rangle\langle D_{j}\rangle=N_{F}\mu_{\rm x}\langle{\tilde{L}}\rangle,

\displaystyle\langle\Sigma_{D}\rangle\approx N_{F}\mu_{\rm x}\,\hat{m}\Bigl[\,1-\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{N_{c}-N_{F}}\frac{\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c}-N_{F})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle^{2}}{\hat{m}^{2}}\,\Bigr]\approx N_{F}\mu_{\rm x}\hat{m}\Bigl[\,1-\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{N_{c}-N_{F}}\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{2}}{{\hat{m}}}\Bigr)^{\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{N_{c}-N_{F}}}\Bigr]\,,

\langle S\rangle_{N_{F}}=0,\hskip 11.74988pt\langle{\overline{D}}_{j}\rangle\langle D_{j}\rangle\approx\mu_{\rm x}\,\hat{m}\Bigl[\,1-\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{N_{c}-N_{F}}\frac{\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c}-N_{F})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle^{2}}{\hat{m}^{2}}\,\Bigr]-\mu_{\rm x}\langle\Lambda_{SU(N_{F})}\rangle{\tilde{\rho}}^{\,{j-1}}\,,\hskip 11.74988ptj=1...N_{F}\,.

\approx N_{F}\mu_{\rm x}\,\hat{m}\Bigl[\,1-\frac{N_{c}}{N_{c}-N_{F}}\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{2}}{{\hat{m}}}\Bigr)^{\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{N_{c}-N_{F}}}\Bigr]\,,

The curve (

) has
N_{c}-1

SU(N_{F})
sector already at very small
\mu_{\rm x}:\,\langle\Lambda_{SU(N_{F})}\rangle^{2}/\hat{m}<\mu_{\rm x}\ll\langle\Lambda_{SU(N_{F})}\rangle\ll\Lambda_{2}\ll m
. Instead of higgsed
\langle X^{adj}_{SU(N_{F})}\rangle\sim\langle\Lambda_{SU(N_{F})}\rangle,\,\,SU(N_{F})\rightarrow U^{N_{F}-1}(1)
and subsequent condensation of
N_{F}

dyons
D_{j}
(
47.7
), all
N_{F}
flavors of original electric quarks

{\overline{Q}}^{\,a}_{i},\,Q^{i}_{a},\,\,a,i=1...N_{F}
are then higgsed as

\langle{\overline{Q}}^{\,a}_{i}\rangle=\langle Q_{a}^{i}\rangle\approx\delta^{i}_{a}(\mu_{\rm x}\,\hat{m})^{1/2}>\langle\Lambda_{SU(N_{F})}\rangle
(and clearly
not confined but screened), forming
N_{F}^{2}
long
{\cal N}=2

multiplets of massive   gluons.

Remind that, at
N_{F}<N_{c}
and
m\gg\Lambda_{2}
, the mass spectra in these
S vacua with the multiplicity
N_{c}-N_{F}
, as well as in the SYM vacua
with the multiplicity
N_{c}
in section 44, depend essentially on the
value of
m/\Lambda_{2}
, and all these vacua with the unbroken global flavor
symmetry
U(N_{F})
evolve at
\mu_{\rm x}\ll m\ll\Lambda_{2}
to L-vacua with the
multiplicity
2N_{c}-N_{F}
and with the spontaneously broken discrete

Z_{2N_{c}-N_{F}}
symmetry, see sections 3.1 and 12 in
[
17
]
.

48

Conclusions to Part III

We presented above in this Part III the calculations of
mass spectra of softly broken
{\cal N}=2\,\,SU(N_{c})
or
U(N_{c})

gauge theories in a large number of their various vacua with the
unbroken non-trivial
Z_{2N_{c}-N_{F}\geq 2}
discrete symmetry. The quantum
numbers of light particles in each vacuum were determined, as well as
forms of corresponding low energy Lagrangians and mass spectra at
different hierarchies between the Lagrangian parameters

m,\,\mu_{\rm x},\,\Lambda_{2}
. A crucial role in obtaining these results was played
by the use : a) the unbroken
Z_{2N_{c}-N_{F}\geq 2}
symmetry,  b) the
pattern of spontaneous flavor symmetry breaking,
U(N_{F})\rightarrow U({\rm n}_{1})\times U({\rm n}_{2}),\,0\leq{\rm n}_{1}<N_{F}/2,\,
c) the knowledge of
multiplicities of various vacua. Besides, the knowledge of the quark
and gluino condensates

\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{N_{c}},\,\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{N_{c}}
,
and
\langle S\rangle_{N_{c}}
(obtained from the effective superpotential

{\mathcal{W}}_{\rm tot}^{\rm eff}
) was also of great importance. In addition, two
dynamical
assumptions
”A” and ”B ” of general character
formulated in section 40 were used. They concern the BPS properties
of original particles and the absence of extra non-BPS fields massless
at
\mu_{\rm x}\neq 0
. All this appeared to be sufficient to
calculate mass spectra in vacua considered at small but nonzero
values   of
\mu_{\rm x}
, in particular at
0<\mu_{\rm x}<M_{\rm min}
, where
M_{\rm min}
is the independent of
\mu_{\rm x}
mass scale appropriate for
each given vacuum,   such that the phase and mass
spectrum continue smoothly down to

\mu_{\rm x}\rightarrow 0
. In other words, the theory stays at
\mu_{\rm x}<M_{\rm min}

in the same regime and all hierarchies in the mass spectrum are the
same as in the
{\cal N}=2
theory at
\mu_{\rm x}\rightarrow 0
.

Within this framework we considered first in detail in section 41.1 the   br2
vacua of
SU(N_{c})
theory with
N_{c}<N_{F}<2N_{c}-1
(these are vacua
of the baryonic branch in
[
22
]
or zero vacua in
[
40
,
41
]
),
at
m\ll\Lambda_{2}
and
0<\mu_{\rm x}\ll M^{(\rm br2)}_{\rm min}=\langle\Lambda^{SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle^{2}/\Lambda_{2}\ll\langle\Lambda^{SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle\ll m\ll\Lambda_{2}
. The original color symmetry is
broken   spontaneously in these vacua by higgsed
\langle X^{adj}_{SU(N_{c})}\rangle\neq 0
in three stages. The first stage
is at   the highest scale
\mu\sim\Lambda_{2}
,
\,\langle X^{adj}_{SU(2N_{c}-N_{F})}\rangle\sim\Lambda_{2}
,
\,\,SU(N_{c})\rightarrow SU(N_{F}-N_{c})\times U^{(1)}(1)\times U^{2N_{c}-N_{F}-1}(1)
, this pattern is
required by the unbroken discrete
Z_{2N_{c}-N_{F}\geq 2}
symmetry and is
necessary to avoid
g^{2}(\mu<\Lambda_{2})<0
in this effectively massless at the
scale
\mu\sim\Lambda_{2}

\,\,{\cal N}=2
UV free theory. The second stage
is in the
SU(N_{F}-N_{c})
color sector at the scale
\mu\sim m
,

\,\langle X^{adj}_{SU(N_{F}-N_{c})}\rangle\sim m\ll\Lambda_{2}
,

\,SU(N_{F}-N_{c})\rightarrow SU({\rm n}_{1})\times U^{(2)}(1)\times SU(N_{F}-N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})
. And
the third stage is in the
SU(N_{F}-N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})
color sector at the scale

\mu\sim\langle\Lambda^{SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle\ll m
,

\,SU(N_{F}-N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})\rightarrow U^{N_{F}-N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}-1}(1)
.

The global flavor symmetry
U(N_{F})
is broken spontaneously at

\mu_{\rm x}\neq 0
in these br2-vacua at the scale
\mu\sim(\mu_{\rm x}m)^{1/2}

in   the
SU({\rm n}_{1})
color sector by higgsed original pure electric quarks,

\,\langle{\overline{Q}}^{\,a}_{i}\rangle=\langle Q^{i}_{a}\rangle\sim\delta_{a}^{i}\,(\mu_{\rm x}m)^{1/2}\,,\,\,a=1...{\rm n}_{1},\,\,i=1...N_{F}
, as :

U(N_{F})\rightarrow U({\rm n}_{1})\times U({\rm n}_{2}),\,1\leq{\rm n}_{1}<N_{F}-N_{c}
.

It was shown that the lightest charged BPS particles (massless at

\mu_{\rm x}\rightarrow 0
) are the following. -

1)
2N_{c}-N_{F}
flavorless mutually local dyons
D_{j},{\overline{D}}_{j}
(this number

2N_{c}-N_{F}
is required by the unbroken
Z_{2N_{c}-N_{F}\geq 2}
discrete symmetry
which operates interchanging them among each other), with the two
nonzero pure electric charges, the
SU(N_{c})
baryon charge and

U^{(1)}(1)
one. The nonzero magnetic charges of all these dyons are
corresponding
SU(2N_{c}-N_{F})
adjoints. The whole
2N_{c}-N_{F}
set of these dyons
is coupled with
2N_{c}-N_{F}
Abelian
{\cal N}=2
photon multiplets,

U^{2N_{c}-N_{F}-1}(1)
magnetic and
U^{(1)}(1)
electric. These dyons are
formed at the scale
\mu\sim\Lambda_{2}
, at the first stage of the color
breaking, and are mutually local with respect to all particles of the

SU(N_{F}-N_{c})
sector and between themselves.

2) All original electric particles of remaining unbroken at
\mu_{\rm x}\rightarrow 0
\,SU({\rm n}_{1})
subgroup of original
SU(N_{c})
.

3)
{\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}-1
pure magnetic monopoles with the
SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})

adjoint charges. These last are formed at the low scale

\mu\sim\langle\Lambda^{SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle\ll m\ll\Lambda_{2}
in the
{\cal N}=2\,\,SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})

SYM   sector, with the scale factor
\langle\Lambda^{SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle
of its gauge coupling. These
magnetic monopoles are coupled with
U^{{\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}-1}(1)

{\cal N}=2
Abelian magnetic photon multiplets.

4) In addition to original electric
{\cal N}=2\,\,SU({\rm n}_{1})
adjoints,
there are magnetic
U^{{\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}-1}(1)
and
U^{2N_{c}-N_{F}-1}(1)
, and one

U^{(1)}(1)
electric
{\cal N}=2
Abelian photon multiplets, all
massless at
\mu_{\rm x}\rightarrow 0
.

Massless at
\mu_{\rm x}\rightarrow 0

\,2N_{c}-N_{F}
dyons
D_{j},\,{\rm n}_{1}
original pure
electric quarks
Q^{i}_{a},{\overline{Q}}^{a}_{i},\,a=1...{\rm n}_{1},\,i=1...N_{F}
from

SU({\rm n}_{1})
, and
{\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}-1
pure magnetic monopoles are all higgsed
at small
\mu_{\rm x}\neq 0
, and no massless particles remains (except for

2{\rm n}_{1}{\rm n}_{2}
Nambu-Goldstone multiplets due to spontaneous breaking
of global flavor symmetry,
U(N_{F})\rightarrow U({\rm n}_{1})\times U({\rm n}_{2})
) in the

SU({\rm n}_{1})
color sector due to higgsed original pure electric quarks
with
N_{F}
flavors). The mass spectrum was described in these
br2-vacua at smallest
0<\mu_{\rm x}\ll\langle\Lambda^{SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle^{2}/\Lambda_{2}
.

The material of this section 41.1 served then as a basis for similar
regimes in sections 42, 45, 46, and 47.

The mass parameter
\mu_{\rm x}
was increased then in two different
stages :  1)
\langle\Lambda^{SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle\ll\mu_{\rm x}\ll m
in section 41.3 ;  2)

m\ll\mu_{\rm x}\ll\Lambda_{2}
in section 41.4, and changes in the mass spectra were
described. In all those cases when the corresponding
{\cal N}=1
SQCD
lower energy theories were weakly coupled, we needed no additional
dynamical assumptions. And only in those few cases in section 41.4,
when the corresponding
{\cal N}=1
SQCD theories were in the strongly
coupled conformal regime at
3N_{c}/2<N_{F}<2N_{c}
, we used additionally
the assumption of the dynamical scenario introduced in
[
20
]
.

In essence, this assumption from
[
20
]
looks here as follows :

no additional parametrically light composite solitons are formed in
this
{\cal N}=1\,\,SU({\overline{N}}_{c})
SQCD without decoupled colored adjoint
scalars
X^{\rm adj}_{SU({\overline{N}}_{c})}
at those even lower scales where this

{\cal N}=1
conformal regime is broken by nonzero particle masses

(see also the footnote
51
). As was shown in

[
20
,
17
,
26
]
,
this dynamical scenario does not contradict to any
proven properties of
{\cal N}=1
SQCD and satisfies all those checks of
Seiberg’s duality hypothesis for
{\cal N}=1
SQCD which were used in

[
5
]
, and allows to calculate the corresponding mass spectra.

Many other vacua with the broken or unbroken flavor symmetry and at
different hierarchies between the Lagrangian parameters
m,\,\mu_{\rm x},\,\Lambda_{2}
were considered in sections 42-47 and corresponding mass spectra
were calculated within the above described framework. Besides,
calculations of power corrections to the leading terms of the low
energy condensates of original quarks
Q,\,{\overline{Q}}
and dyons in a
number of vacua are presented in two important Appendices A and B.
The results
agree with   also presented in these Appendices
independent

calculations of these quark and dyon condensates using roots of the
Seiberg-Witten spectral curve. And because these last calculations
with roots are valid
only for BPS particles, these agreements
serve as an independent confirmation of the main assumption ”A” from
Introduction about BPS properties of original quarks, and serve as
numerous checks of a self-consistency of the whole approach
.

We consider that there is no need to repeat in detail in these
conclusions all results obtained in this paper (see the table of
contents). But at least two points have to be emphasized.

1) As shown e.g. in section 45.2 (and similarly in sections 45.4, 46 and 47),
the widespread opinion that the holomorphic dependence of gauge invariant
chiral condensates (e.g.
\langle(({\overline{Q}}Q))_{1,2}\rangle_{N_{c}},\,\langle S\rangle_{N_{c}},\,\\
\langle{\rm Tr\,}(X^{adj}_{SU(N_{c})})^{2}\rangle\,)
on chiral parameters
of the superpotential implies the absence of phase
transitions in supersymmetric theories is not right.

2) In contradiction with the smooth analytical crossover transition from
the   higher energy region to lower energy one (in sections 45,  46 and 47)
and the emergence of the ”instead-of-confinement” regime proclaimed in
a number of papers by M.Shifman and A.Yung, see e.g. the latest paper

[
48
]
and references therein to their previous papers with this
crossover, we have found that this transition is not an analytic crossover
but a   phase transition. See the critique of the M.Shifman and A.Yung
proposal in e.g. sections 45.2, 45.4 and in section 8 of
[
25
]
.

In particular, the arguments in
[
37
]
in favour of crossover are based
on considering the unequal mass quarks with
\Delta m\neq 0
, the
“outside”  region
\Delta m\gg\Lambda_{2}
and “inside”  region

\Delta m\ll\Lambda_{2}
and the path between them going through
Argyres-Douglas point.

We would like to emphasize that these “outside”  and “inside”
regions are
completely separated
by so-called “wall crossing curves”.
And there is no such pass in the complex plane
\Delta m/\Lambda_{2}

which connects the “outside”  and “inside”  regions and does
not cross this “wall crossing curve”. And the path going through
Argyres-Douglas point is not the exception.

The quantum numbers of light particles jump generically when
the generic path crosses the “wall crossing curve” and have to be
carefully considered separately in the “outside”  and “inside”
regions.

And besides, as for the example considered in
[
37
]
in support of
crossover between the confinement and higgs regimes (and
proposal of “instead of confinement”  regime), i.e.
N_{c}=3,\,N_{F}=5,\,2N_{c}-N_{F}=1
. It is the example with the trivial
Z_{2N_{c}-N_{F}=1}

symmetry which gives no restrictions. It belongs
to another type of vacua, see section 45.5.
And this clearly invalidates all statements of M. Shifman and
A.Yung about quantum numbers of light particles, about crossover
and about “instead of confinement” regime in vacua with
non-trivial unbroken
Z_{2N_{c}-N_{F}\geq 2}
symmetry.

Appendices.  Quark and dyon condensates

As examples, we present below in two important
Appendices the
calculation of leading power corrections to the low energy quark
condensates
\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{{\rm n}_{1}}
in br1 vacua of
SU(N_{c})
and

U(N_{c})
in sections 43.1 and 43.2, and in br2 vacua of
SU(N_{c})
and

U(N_{c})
in sections 41.1 and 41.2, and to the dyon condensates in
sections 41.1 and 41.2. The results agree with also presented in these
Appendices
independent
calculations of these condensates using
roots of the Seiberg-Witten spectral curve. T͡hese agreements serve as
the independent numerous checks of a few used assumptions and
of self-consistency of the whole approach.

Appendix AW

Calculations of quark condensates

1) br1 vacua of
SU(N_{c}),\,\,m\gg\Lambda_{2}
in section 43.1

From (
40.3
),(
40.4
), in these vacua (up to even smaller
power corrections)

\displaystyle\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{N_{c}}=\mu_{\rm x}m_{3}-\frac{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{2}}{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{N_{c}}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{N_{c}}\approx\mu_{\rm x}m_{3}\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{2}}{m_{3}}\Bigr)^{\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}}\,,

(AW.1)

\langle S\rangle_{N_{c}}=\frac{\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{N_{c}}\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{N_{c}}}{\mu_{\rm x}}\approx\,m_{3}\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{N_{c}}\,,\hskip 11.74988ptm_{3}=\frac{N_{c}}{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}m\,.

From (
41.1.9
),(
AW.1
), the leading power correction to

\langle{\mathcal{W}}^{\,\rm eff}_{\rm tot}\rangle
is

\displaystyle\langle\delta{\mathcal{W}}^{\,\rm eff}_{\rm tot}\rangle\approx(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})\mu_{\rm x}m^{2}_{3}\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{2}}{m_{3}}\Bigr)^{\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}}\approx(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})\langle S\rangle_{N_{c}}\,.

(AW.2)

On the other hand, this leading power correction to
\langle{\mathcal{W}}^{\,\rm low}_{\rm tot}\rangle
originates from the
SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})
SYM part,
see   (
43.1.2
),(
43.1.4
),

\langle\delta{\mathcal{W}}^{\,\rm low}_{\rm tot}\rangle\approx\mu_{\rm x}\langle\,{\rm Tr\,}\Bigl(X^{adj}_{SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}\Bigr)^{2}\,\rangle\approx(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})\langle S\rangle_{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}\approx(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})\mu_{\rm x}\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle^{2}\approx

\displaystyle\approx(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})\mu_{\rm x}m_{3}^{2}\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{2}}{m_{3}}\Bigr)^{\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\rightarrow\hskip 11.74988pt\langle\delta{\mathcal{W}}^{\,\rm low}_{\rm tot}\rangle=\langle\delta{\mathcal{W}}^{\,\rm eff}_{\rm tot}\rangle\,,

(AW.3)

as it should be.

To calculate the leading power correction

\delta\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{{\rm n}_{1}}\sim\mu_{\rm x}\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle^{2}/m
to the low energy
quark condensate
\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{{\rm n}_{1}}
, we have to account for
the   terms with   the first power of quantum fluctuation
{\hat{a}}

of the field
a=\langle a\rangle+{\hat{a}},\,\,\langle{\hat{a}}\rangle=0
,
in the
SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})
SYM contribution to
\delta{\mathcal{W}}^{\,\rm low}_{\rm tot}
, see (
43.1.2
)-(
43.1.4
),

\delta{\mathcal{W}}^{\,\rm low}_{\rm tot}\approx(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})\mu_{\rm x}\,\delta\,\Bigl(\Lambda^{SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\Bigr)^{2}\approx(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})\mu_{\rm x}\,\delta\,\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{2}^{2N_{c}-N_{F}}(m_{3}-c\,{\hat{a}})^{N_{F}}}{[\,m_{3}+(1-c){\hat{a}}\,]^{2{\rm n}_{1}}}\Bigr)^{\frac{1}{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}}\approx

\displaystyle\approx\,\mu_{\rm x}\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle^{2}\,\Bigl[\,-{\rm n}_{1}\frac{(2N_{c}-N_{F})}{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}\,\Bigr]\,\frac{{\hat{a}}}{m_{3}}\,,\hskip 11.74988ptm_{3}=\frac{N_{c}}{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}m\,.

(AW.4)

As a result, from (
43.1.4
),(
AW.4
),(
AW.1
),

\delta\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{{\rm n}_{1}}\approx\frac{1}{{\rm n}_{1}}\langle\frac{\partial}{\partial{\hat{a}}}\delta{\mathcal{W}}^{\,\rm low}_{\rm tot}\rangle\approx-\frac{(2N_{c}-N_{F})}{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}\,\mu_{\rm x}\frac{\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle^{2}}{m_{3}}\,,

\displaystyle\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle^{SU(N_{c})}_{{\rm n}_{1}}=\langle{\overline{Q}}^{1}_{1}\rangle\langle Q^{1}_{1}\rangle\approx\mu_{\rm x}m_{3}\Biggl[1-\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}\,\frac{\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle^{2}}{m^{2}_{3}}\Biggr]\approx

(AW.5)

\approx\mu_{\rm x}m_{3}\Biggl[1-\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{2}}{m_{3}}\Bigr)^{\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}}\Biggr]\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle^{SU(N_{c})}_{N_{c}}\approx\mu_{\rm x}m_{3}\Biggl[1-\frac{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{2}}{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{2}}{m_{3}}\Bigr)^{\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}}\Biggr]\,.

It is seen from (
AW.5
) that, although the leading terms are
the   same in
\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{{\rm n}_{1}}
and
\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{N_{c}}
, the leading power corrections are different.

2) br1 vacua of
U(N_{c}),\,\,m\gg\Lambda_{2}
in section 43.2

From (

),(

\displaystyle\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{N_{c}}=\mu_{\rm x}m-\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{N_{c}}\,,

\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{N_{c}}=\mu_{\rm x}\Lambda_{2}^{\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}}\Bigl(\frac{\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{1}\rangle_{N_{c}}}{\mu_{\rm x}}\Bigr)^{\frac{{\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}}\approx\mu_{\rm x}m\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{2}}{m}\Bigr)^{\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}}\Biggl(1+O\Bigl(\frac{\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle^{2}}{m^{2}}\Bigr)\Biggr)\,,

\displaystyle\frac{\langle a_{0}\rangle}{m}=\frac{1}{N_{c}\mu_{\rm x}m}\langle{\rm Tr}\,({\overline{q}}q)\rangle_{N_{c}}\approx\frac{{\rm n}_{1}}{N_{c}}+\frac{{\rm n}_{2}-{\rm n}_{1}}{N_{c}}\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{2}}{m}\Bigr)^{\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}}\,,\hskip 11.74988pt\langle a\rangle=\langle m-a_{0}\rangle\,,

(AW.7)

\displaystyle\langle{\mathcal{W}}^{\,\rm low}_{\rm tot}\rangle\approx\Bigl[\langle{\mathcal{W}}_{{\rm n}_{1}}\rangle=0\,\Bigr]+\Biggl[\langle{\mathcal{W}}_{a_{0},a}\rangle=\frac{\mu_{\rm x}}{2}\Bigl(\,N_{c}\langle a_{0}\rangle^{2}+\frac{{\rm n}_{1}N_{c}}{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}\langle a\rangle^{2}\Bigr)\Biggr]+\langle{\mathcal{W}}_{SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}^{\,SYM}\rangle\,,

\approx(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})\mu_{\rm x}\langle\Biggl(\frac{\Lambda_{2}^{2N_{c}-N_{F}}(m-a_{0}-c\,a)^{N_{F}}}{[\,(1-c)a\,]^{2{\rm n}_{1}}}\Biggr)^{\frac{1}{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}}\rangle\approx(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})\mu_{\rm x}\,m^{2}\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{2}}{m}\Bigr)^{\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}}\,.

From (
AW.6
)-(

), the leading power correction

\langle\,\delta\,{\mathcal{W}}^{\,\rm low}_{\rm tot}\rangle\sim\mu_{\rm x}\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle^{2}
to
\langle{\mathcal{W}}^{\,\rm low}_{\rm tot}\rangle
looks as

\displaystyle\approx(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})\mu_{\rm x}\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle^{2}\approx(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})\,m\langle({\overline{Q}}Q)_{2}\rangle_{N_{c}}\approx(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})\mu_{\rm x}m^{2}\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{2}}{m}\Bigr)^{\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}}\,.

(AW.9)

Instead of (
41.1.9
),(

) we have now, see
(

section 43.2, there are now in addition massless dyons in br2 vacua, to

). The sum rule

) looks in these br2 vacua as, see (

),

\displaystyle N_{c}\langle a_{0}\rangle\approx\Bigl({\rm n}_{2}m+({\rm n}_{1}-{\rm n}_{2})\frac{\langle\Lambda^{SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle^{2}}{m}\Bigr)\approx\Biggl({\rm n}_{2}m+({\rm n}_{1}-{\rm n}_{2})m\Bigl(\frac{m}{\Lambda_{2}}\Bigr)^{\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{{\rm n}_{2}-N_{c}}}\Biggr)\approx

(AX.1)

\approx\Bigl({\rm n}_{1}m+({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}-1)A^{(M)}_{c}\frac{\langle\Lambda^{SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle^{2}}{m}-\frac{1}{2}(e^{+}+e^{-}=0)+(2N_{c}-N_{F})C^{(D)}_{c}\Bigr)\,,

where
{\rm n}_{1}(-e^{(Q)}_{i})={\rm n}_{1}m
is the contribution of
{\rm n}_{1}
equal
double roots of original electric quarks from
SU({\rm n}_{1})
, the term
with

is the contribution from the center of
{\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}-1

unequal   double   roots of magnetic monopoles, and the term with

is the contribution from the center of
2N_{c}-N_{F}
unequal
double roots of   dyons. We obtain then from the curve (
40.2
)
togetherwith (
AX.1
) (with our accuracy)

\displaystyle A^{(M)}_{c}=\frac{{\rm n}_{2}-{\rm n}_{1}}{{\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}-1}\,,\hskip 11.74988ptC^{(D)}_{c}=\frac{{\rm n}_{2}-{\rm n}_{1}}{2N_{c}-N_{F}}m\Bigl(1-2\frac{\langle\Lambda^{SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle^{2}}{m^{2}}\Bigr)\,,\,\,\,

(AX.2)

e^{(M)}_{k}\approx 2\cos(\frac{\pi k}{{\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}})\langle\Lambda^{SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle-\frac{{\rm n}_{2}-{\rm n}_{1}}{{\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}-1}\frac{\langle\Lambda^{SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle^{2}}{m}\,,\hskip 11.74988ptk=1...({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}-1)\,,

e^{(D)}_{j}\approx\omega^{j-1}\Lambda_{2}\Biggl(1+O\Bigl(\frac{m}{\Lambda_{2}}\Bigr)^{2N_{c}-N_{F}}\Biggr)-\frac{{\rm n}_{2}-{\rm n}_{1}}{2N_{c}-N_{F}}\,m\Biggl(1-2\Bigl(\frac{m}{\Lambda_{2}}\Bigr)^{\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{{\rm n}_{2}-N_{c}}}\Biggr)\,,\,\,j=1...(2N_{c}-N_{F})\,,

while
e^{\pm}
are given in (

).

From (

),(

) we obtain now for the dyon

at

\displaystyle\langle{\overline{D}}_{j}D_{j}\rangle_{U(N_{c})}=\langle{\overline{D}}_{j}\rangle\langle D_{j}\rangle\approx\mu_{\rm x}\Biggl[\,-\,\omega^{j-1}\Lambda_{2}+\frac{{\rm n}_{2}-{\rm n}_{1}}{2N_{c}-N_{F}}\,m\Biggl(1-2\Bigl(\frac{m}{\Lambda_{2}}\Bigr)^{\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{{\rm n}_{2}-N_{c}}}\Biggr)\Biggr]\,,

(AX.3)

\langle\Sigma^{U(N_{c})}_{D}\rangle\equiv\sum_{j=1}^{2N_{c}-N_{F}}\langle{\overline{D}}_{j}D_{j}\rangle_{U(N_{c})}=\sum_{j=1}^{2N_{c}-N_{F}}\langle{\overline{D}}_{j}\rangle\langle D_{j}\rangle\approx\mu_{\rm x}m\,({\rm n}_{2}-{\rm n}_{1})\Biggl(1-2\Bigl(\frac{m}{\Lambda_{2}}\Bigr)^{\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{{\rm n}_{2}-N_{c}}}\Biggr),\,\,\,j=1...2N_{c}-N_{F}\,.

\sim\mu_{\rm x}m

\displaystyle\langle\frac{\partial}{\partial a_{0}}{\mathcal{W}}^{\,\rm low}_{\rm tot}\rangle=0=-\langle{\rm Tr\,}({\overline{q}}q)\rangle_{{\rm n}_{1}}-\langle\Sigma^{U(N_{c})}_{D}\rangle+\mu_{\rm x}N_{c}\langle a_{0}\rangle-\mu_{\rm x}N_{c}\delta_{3}\langle a_{1}\rangle=-\mu_{\rm x}N_{c}\delta_{3}\langle a_{1}\rangle\rightarrow{\boldsymbol{\delta}}{\mathbf{{}_{3}=0}}.\,\,\hskip 11.74988pt

\hskip-8.53581pt\langle\frac{\partial}{\partial{\hat{a}}_{0}}\delta{\mathcal{W}}^{\,\rm low}_{\rm tot}\rangle=0\rightarrow\delta\langle\Sigma^{U(N_{c})}_{D}\rangle\approx\Bigl[-\delta\langle{\rm Tr}({\overline{q}}q)\rangle_{{\rm n}_{1}}=2{\rm n}_{1}\mu_{\rm x}m\Bigl(\frac{m}{\Lambda_{2}}\Bigr)^{\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{{\rm n}_{2}-N_{c}}}\Bigr]_{Q}+\Bigl[({\rm n}_{1}-{\rm n}_{2})\mu_{\rm x}m\Bigl(\frac{m}{\Lambda_{2}}\Bigr)^{\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{{\rm n}_{2}-N_{c}}}\Bigr]_{\delta{\mathcal{W}}_{a_{0}}}\,\,\,

\displaystyle+\Bigl[\langle\frac{\partial}{\partial{\hat{a}}_{0}}\delta{\mathcal{W}}_{SU({\overline{N}}_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}^{\,SYM}\rangle=-N_{F}\mu_{\rm x}m\Bigl(\frac{m}{\Lambda_{2}}\Bigr)^{\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{{\rm n}_{2}-N_{c}}}\Bigr]_{\delta{\mathcal{W}}_{SYM}}\approx-2\mu_{\rm x}m\,({\rm n}_{2}-{\rm n}_{1})\Bigl(\frac{m}{\Lambda_{2}}\Bigr)^{\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{{\rm n}_{2}-N_{c}}}\,,

\displaystyle\approx\mu_{\rm x}\,\hat{m}({\rm n}_{2}-{\rm n}_{1})\Biggl[1+\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{{\rm n}_{2}-N_{c}}\Bigl(\frac{\hat{m}}{\Lambda_{2}}\Bigr)^{\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{{\rm n}_{2}-N_{c}}}\Biggr]\,,

\displaystyle e^{\pm}_{c}=\frac{1}{2}(e^{+}+e^{-})\approx m_{3}\Biggl[\,\frac{{\rm n}_{1}}{N_{c}}+\frac{{\rm n}_{2}-{\rm n}_{1}}{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}\frac{\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle^{2}}{m^{2}_{3}}\,\Biggr],\hskip 11.74988ptm_{3}=\frac{N_{c}}{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}m\,,

(AX.9)

e^{(M)}_{k}\approx\Biggl[\,e^{\pm}_{c}-\frac{{\rm n}_{2}-{\rm n}_{1}}{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}-1}\frac{\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle^{2}}{m_{3}}\,\Biggr]+2\cos(\frac{\pi k}{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}})\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle,\hskip 11.74988pt\frac{1}{2}\sum_{n=1}^{2N_{c}}(-e_{n})=0\,.

\displaystyle\cdot\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{2}}{m_{3}}\Bigr)^{\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}}\Biggr)\,\Biggr]\approx m_{3}\Biggl[\frac{{\rm n}_{1}}{N_{c}}+\frac{{\rm n}_{2}-{\rm n}_{1}}{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}\Bigl(\frac{\Lambda_{2}}{m_{3}}\Bigr)^{\frac{2N_{c}-N_{F}}{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}}\Biggr]\approx m_{3}\Biggl[\,\frac{{\rm n}_{1}}{N_{c}}+\frac{{\rm n}_{2}-{\rm n}_{1}}{N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1}}\frac{\langle\Lambda^{SU(N_{c}-{\rm n}_{1})}_{{\cal N}=2\,\,SYM}\rangle^{2}}{m^{2}_{3}}\,\Biggr]\,.

independent

(AX.14)

independent
